# Translator's Preface

*Sri Chaitanya-mangala* is a Bengali poem on the life and pastimes of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu. Its author, Lochana Dasa Thakura, was a disciple of Narahari Sarakara Thakura of Shrikhanda, an associate of the Lord; in the poem he repeatedly calls himself a follower of Narahari. According to Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati (see his preface below), Lochana Dasa came of a vaidya family of the Rarh country and was born in the village of Kogram near Katwa (Burdwan district). The poem was probably written about the middle of the sixteenth century (it is usually placed in the 1550s–1560s); no exact date is known.

The basis of the poem, as the author himself says (Sutra-khanda, vv. 53–56), is Murari Gupta's Sanskrit poem on the Lord, composed as a dialogue between Murari and Damodara Pandita (the so-called *Kadacha* of Murari Gupta; Bhaktisiddhanta calls it *Sri Chaitanya-charita*). Lochana Dasa retells it "as a sung narrative", a *panchali*: the poem is divided into songs, each with its raga and many with a *disha* (refrain); the main metre is the *payar* (couplet), and *tripadi* also occurs in the songs. Many places are enlarged with traditions not found in Murari. The poem has four parts (khandas): the Sutra-khanda (introduction and the "prehistory" of the Lord's descent), the Adi-khanda (from His birth to the journey to Gaya), the Madhya-khanda (from the return from Gaya to His coming to Puri and the meeting with Sarvabhauma) and the Shesha-khanda (the southern tour, Vrindavana, the return to Nilachala, the last pastimes).

**The base text.** The translation follows the edition of Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati (Sri Chaitanya Math, Mayapur, 1922)[^00-1]. His text and his numbering of the couplets are the basis of our translation. The prose summaries which Bhaktisiddhanta placed at the head of chapters and sections are translated and given at the head of the corresponding sections ("Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati"). The scan of this edition available to us is badly damaged in places; gaps and obscure places have been restored from two other editions: that of Haridasa Shastri (Vrindavana, 1983; the Bengali text is printed there in Devanagari script) and that of Atulakrishna Gosvami (Calcutta, 1903). Where the editions differ in sense, we follow Bhaktisiddhanta and note significant differences in the footnotes.

**Text not found in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition.** The 1983 edition contains a good deal that is absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition: whole chapters, passages and single couplets. All of this has been translated and put in its place in the text (following the 1983 edition), numbered "80+1", "80+2" and so on (that is, after v. 80 of Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); at the start of each such insertion a footnote says that it is absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition and whether the 1903 edition has it. Translated in this way are, for example, the section on Shachi's grief over the brahmana's curse (Madhya-khanda, portion 33), the section "Consoling the devotees" (portion 38), many stanzas about the women of Nadia (portions 18–22) and others. Where the 1983 edition does not add text but gives a different redaction of the same passage, the main text follows Bhaktisiddhanta and the other redaction is given in translation in a footnote. Bhaktisiddhanta's edition ends with the story of the poor brahmana from Dravida and Vibhishana; the last chapter, on the Lord's disappearance (ch. 4 of the Shesha-khanda in the 1983 edition), and Lochana Dasa's closing words about himself exist only in the editions of 1983 and 1903; they are translated as an insertion of the same kind, with their own footnote (portion 52)[^00-2].

**Faithfulness to the author.** We translate Lochana Dasa's words as they stand, without correcting them to later theology and without softening them. Our authority in interpretation is Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati. Where Lochana Dasa departs from the Gaudiya siddhanta as he taught it — for example, in the lines about the women of Nadia drawn to Gaura as to a *nagara* (nagari-bhava), in some identifications of the Lord's associates, in details of the narrative that differ from the *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, in the account of the Lord's disappearance by entering Jagannatha — we give an exact translation and explain the difference in a footnote. Bhaktisiddhanta himself, in his preface, declines to ascribe the "Gaura-nagari" doctrine to the poem and warns against songs circulating under Lochana Dasa's name into which that doctrine was later introduced.

**Arrangement of the translation.** The couplets are numbered as in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition; at the start of some chapters and sections the edition's numbering begins anew, and so does ours. Where the edition repeats numbers by mistake, we keep the printed numbers and distinguish the repeats by a letter (371a; 393a–401b in portion 13, see footnote 1 there). Lines naming the raga and the refrain (disha) are given in italics without a number; words added for clarity are in square brackets. A tripadi stanza is given under one number. The translation is divided into 52 portions: Sutra-khanda 01–08, Adi-khanda 09–23, Madhya-khanda 24–45, Shesha-khanda 46–52. The full edition contains, besides the translation, the Bengali original (in Bengali script, in the medieval spelling of the edition), its Latin transliteration and a word-for-word translation. Modern English translations were consulted only for doubtful places.

There follow Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati's preface to his edition and his synopsis of the contents by parts and chapters (*Khanda-vivarana*).

# Preface by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati

*Preface (bhumika) to Sri Chaitanya-mangala*[^00-3]

**The reader's qualification.** In taking up any book one must first of all make the mind [pure and calm], not allow former impressions to prevail, and set about reading without prejudice, in a mood favourable to grasping its meaning. Every author wishes his labour to serve to free the reader from ignorance. On the other hand, the reader too, having considered his own qualification (*adhikara*), should think: "[I wish to read this book] in order to receive light on what I do not know." The pride nursed by lovers of argument, who, regarding themselves as examiners, set out to test the author, is merely the attitude of a trader. If one seeks in reading a calculation of profit, the place [of the truth] is taken by hopes of gain, honour and fame. Worldly people, thus driven by their desires, take the subject the book speaks of for merchandise — and are deprived of [the benefit borne by] the words of the devotee author.

**The meaning of the book's title.** By the pastimes of Sri Chaitanyadeva the bonds will fall from the living beings bewildered by maya and blessing (*mangala*) will come — therefore the narrative of Sri Chaitanya is called *Sri Chaitanya-mangala*. Sri Chaitanyadeva Himself is full of the highest blessing; therefore the narrative of Sri Chaitanya may well be called *Chaitanya-mangala*. Srila Vrindavana Dasa Thakura too wrote a book under this name; Srila Krishnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami Prabhu mentions it in his *Sri Chaitanya-charitamrita*[^00-4]. That very book became famous under the name *Sri Chaitanya-bhagavata*.

Certain literati of Buddhist leanings and scholars attached to them have recently composed, in the guise of a literary monument, a fictitious book, and are trying to prove that it is old. [Certain] Shaiva literati and literati of [other] communities [hostile to bhakti], showing aversion to pure bhakti, have conceived the idea of uprooting pure bhakti by spreading [this book]. [The book] which mundane literati hostile to bhakti pass off as approved by devotees — displaying only contemptible efforts — we do not honour. Because of their Buddhist convictions and godlessness they have no access to the highest goal (*paramartha*); and the fruit of their illegitimate efforts, rooted in the desire to pass off the worthless (*anartha*) as the highest goal, is that the fictitious *Chaitanya-mangala* composed by Jayananda has been called into being[^00-5]. Pure devotees never read such a book and do not, [by accepting it,] bring defilement on themselves. In the *Bhakti-ratnakara* and other books of Sri Narahari Chakravarti there is not a single mention of the *Chaitanya-mangala* of Jayananda and similar books; therefore, [regarding] this book contrary to the truth as the work of a deviant community (*apasampradaya*), we have given it the name *A-chaitanya-mangala*[^00-6]. The admirers of such books may, together with the haters of bhakti, agree to support it, but we pay them our respects from afar.

In studying the life of Sri Chaitanya, let the indisputable books for us be the works of the reliable biographers of Sri Chaitanya — Srila Kavi-karnapura Gosvami, Srila Murari Gupta Vaidya, Srila Krishnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami and Srila Vrindavana Dasa Thakura. In the *Chaitanya-charita-mahakavya*, in the *Gaura-krishnodaya* of the Utkala poet Sri Govinda-deva, in *Sri Chaitanya-mangala* and [similar] books there is no glaring trace of doctrines opposed to bhakti, and therefore those who walk the path of bhakti accept them as evidence without hesitation[^00-7]. But the *Baul-chandrika*, the *[Bhakta]mala* of Laladasa[^00-8], the *Chaitanya-mangala* of Jayananda and some other books, although they are now the chief object of study among literati, are untouchable for us […]: walking the path of bhakti, we dare not accept them. Those who have realized even a little of the nature of bhakti do not leave the path of bhakti, and so in their writings there is no place for the doctrine of the Charvakas, Buddhist beliefs or the dominance of materialism.

Seeing the grace of language of Srila Lochana Dasa Thakura's *Sri Chaitanya-mangala* and his heartfelt love for Sri Gaura, some indeed say that he is inclined to stray somewhat from the truth of pure bhakti. We are not prepared to say so, for "the actions and gestures of a Vaishnava are not understood even by the wise"[^00-9]. May the abominable desire to impute the stench of the Gaura-nagari doctrine to *Sri Chaitanya-mangala* never take hold of our hearts[^00-10].

Our one hope with regard to the readers is that, praying to Sri Chaitanya for mercy, we shall be freed from various considerations full of anarthas and attain blessing. We also hope that the readers will kindly not make common cause with the ten or thirteen deviant communities (*apasampradayas*) who come forward claiming to be called Gaudiyas and offend the feet of the true Gaudiyas[^00-11].

**The source of Sri Chaitanya-mangala** is the *Sri Chaitanya-charita* of Sri Murari Gupta. The author writes in the Sutra-khanda:

> "All that the Lord did from His birth in childhood,
> how from beginning to end He spread prema —
> about all this Damodara Pandita questioned him,
> and he told him everything from beginning to end in order.
> Thus came about the book of Gauranga's deeds, composed in verse —
> the dialogue with Damodara that came from Murari's lips.
> Hearing it, I was filled with love,
> and I tell of Gaura's deeds in a sung narrative, a panchali."[^00-12]

The author of this book is a disciple of Srila Narahari Dasa, an associate of Sri Gaura; he was born in a vaidya family of the Rarh country, at Kogram in the Katwa subdivision of Burdwan district. In many places of the book Thakura Lochana Dasa calls himself a follower of Srila Narahari Sarakara Thakura Mahashaya.

*Sri Chaitanya-mangala* is a book of the panchali kind, that is, a work composed in song-metres of five kinds[^00-13]. Its language shows an abundance of feeling and extraordinary grace. The collections of popular songs that have lately come into circulation under the name "Lochana's panchali" must be approached with special caution: into many of them the stench of the newfangled Gaura-nagari doctrine has been introduced.

Many hesitate to accept the historical statements of *Sri Chaitanya-mangala*: part [of them] has been gathered on the basis of dreams[^00-14], and full regard for the truth of pure bhakti is not seen everywhere. Tradition says that Srila Lochana Dasa Thakura's book was written somewhat before the *Sri Chaitanya-bhagavata* of Thakura Srila Vrindavana Dasa; when Srila Vrindavana's mother said that [that] book had been written earlier, it became necessary to rename the book of Sri Vrindavana Dasa Thakura[^00-15]. No one can doubt the reliability of the geographical indications of *Sri Chaitanya-mangala*. The composition of *Sri Chaitanya-mangala* was begun on the basis of the childhood pastimes of Sri Gaurasundara recorded by Sri Murari Gupta Vaidya in the *Sri Chaitanya-charita*: Lochana Dasa took them as his source-evidence. The clear description in this book of Sri Chaitanyadeva's ideal of renunciation will give joy to the reader, and above all Srila Lochana Thakura's love for Sri Gaura will draw the love of Gaura's devotees — of this there is no doubt. The old panchali poetry of Gunaraja Khan was composed long before this book[^00-16]. The songs of *Sri Chaitanya-mangala* are sung to this day in various places of Rarh, as *jhumur* or the songs of the *Ramayana* are sung. The poetry of *Sri Chaitanya-charitamrita*, founded on Srila Kaviraja Gosvami's description of prema-bhakti, the charming verse of Sri Lochana Dasa Thakura, and the supremely magnanimous grace of language of Sri Vrindavana Dasa Thakura, rooted in pure bhakti, have always increased the bliss of the devotees of Sri Gaura.

# Synopsis of the Khandas (Khanda-vivarana)

*A brief account of the contents by parts and chapters, placed by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati at the beginning of his edition*[^00-17]. *The portions of our translation are given in brackets.*

## Sutra-khanda

**Mangalacharana** (portion 01). Having sung the glory of Sri Krishna Chaitanya, [the author] prays for the mercy first of the Vaishnavas and then of his own gurudeva, Sriman Narahari Thakura; bows at the feet of the countless associates of Gaura-lila, known and unknown; praying for the blessing of guru and Vaishnavas, declares that the model of his book, a *panchali-prabandha*, is the Sanskrit mahakavya of Sri Murari Gupta called *Sri Chaitanya-charita*; and briefly describes what will be written in the Adi-, Madhya- and Shesha-khanda.

**Grantharambha (the beginning of the narrative)** (portions 02–08). At the beginning of the narrative the author, drawing on the dialogue of Narada and Uddhava from the *Jaimini-bharata* as related in the conversation of Damodara and Murari, describes why Sri Krishna descended in the form of Sri Gaura. To relieve the distress of living beings stricken by Kali, Devarshi Narada goes to Dvaraka; there, during a conversation of Rukmini and Krishna, the talk turns to Krishna's accepting the bhava of Radha; hearing this, Rukmini suffers at the prospect of separation; as Radha's greatness is being glorified, Sri Krishna, at the Devarshi's prayer, tells him that He will appear as Gaura in Navadvipa, in the house of Shachi and Jagannatha, and shows him His Gaura form. Having seen the Gaura form, the best of sages, singing of Gaura-lila, goes to Naimisharanya and there, in answer to Uddhava's question, glorifies the Kali-yuga, the essence of all yugas, and the dharma of the age, the sankirtana of Hari's name. Then [Narada] goes to Shambhu, best of Vaishnavas, on Kailasa and [reminds] Parvati of her former [vow] to distribute mahaprasada to all living beings without distinction; for this purpose he tells his own story: how he obtained the [Lord's] mahaprasada through Lakshmi-devi, how he gave that prasada to Shiva, how Parvati [was deprived of it] and made her vow, and how as a result the Lord's coming [was promised] — and he sings of Gaura's future appearance in the Kali-yuga. Then [Narada] goes to Brahma and tells of Gaura's appearance, and Brahma brings forward verses of the *Srimad-Bhagavata* and other [scriptures] that bear witness to this lila. Wandering about, Narada sees the misery of the living beings of Kali and becomes thoughtful; to the pensive sage a voice from heaven announces [the appearance] of the avatara, Jagannatha in Nilachala; the Devarshi goes to Purushottama and from there, at the command of the Lord of the gods, sets out for Goloka: first to Vaikuntha, then higher, to Goloka; there he beholds various lilas, faints at the sight of the Gaura form, and hears the news that [the Lord] will come to earth with all the gods. Coming to Shvetadvipa, he sees the wonderful lila of Sri Balarama, [the Lord in the form of] service; then comes the proposal to the gods to take birth in the world of mortals. [Having described how] Krishna, accepting the bhava and lustre of Srimati Radhika, together with His eternal associates [who will come] with their distinctive natures — Rukmini, Satyabhama and others — and taking up the weapon of the sankirtana of the name, descends as Gaura; Balarama as Nityananda; Shiva as Advaita Prabhu[^00-18]; and the other associates as Murari, Mukunda, Srinivasa, Raya Ramananda, Ishvara Puri and Madhava Puri, [the author] glorifies his own guru, Thakura Narahari, and his nephew Raghunandana.

## Adi-khanda

**Chapter 1. Janma-lila (The Birth)** (portion 09). The appearance of Sri Gaurahari on earth with His associates; the coming of the Parabrahman Narayana, [beyond] the gross and the subtle, into the ocean of Shachi's womb; as the burden grows, the lustre of Shachi-devi's body grows; seeing the wonderful lustre, [people] conclude that some great personality has appeared in Shachi's womb; in the sixth month Advaita Prabhu bows to Shachi's womb and circumambulates it; Brahma, Shiva and other gods come to Shachi's womb and glorify the lila of the Lord, the Giver of prema, who bestows prema never given before; Shachi-devi, seeing this, forgets herself; on the full moon of Phalguna, during the eclipse, amid the sankirtana of Hari's name, Bhagavan Gaurahari descends to earth; the ten directions are filled with bliss; goddesses together with the women [of Nadia] come to Shachi's house to see the moon-like face of Shachi's son; Goloka appears in the house; Jagannatha Mishra and the men and women of Nadia are amazed to see, on the lotus feet of the infant with a lion's neck, an elephant's shoulders and a broad chest, the marks of the flag, thunderbolt and goad and various superhuman marks, and understand that the infant is beyond mortals; the distribution of eight kinds of pulses on the eighth day, the festival on the ninth day; the wholehearted love of the neighbours, men and women, for Shachi's son.

**Chapter 2. Balya-lila (Childhood Pastimes)** (portions 09–13). After six months, Gaurahari's first feeding with rice and name-giving; since at His appearance the whole world was filled with bliss, the wise give Him the name Vishvambhara; He walks about the courtyard holding His father's finger; Gaurasundara in armlets, bracelets and various ornaments: the moon in the sky can dispel only outer darkness, but the moon Gaura dispels the inner darkness of living beings as well; lulling her son to sleep, Shachi-devi sees various gods and goddesses glorifying her son; Gaurahari dances wildly with the gods and goddesses, crying "Radha-Govinda"; the sound of ankle-bells is heard on His bare feet; Gaurasundara takes to childish games with His playmates outside the house; when Shachi-devi tries to catch Him, He runs away, and sometimes in anger spoils everything in the house; having explained to His mother the worthlessness of mundane notions of pure and impure and the like, Gaurasundara instructs her in the transcendental knowledge that Krishna is the Lord of all; sitting in a pit full of pots with leftovers, He instructs His mother; He strikes His mother — at the blow she faints, and He brings a coconut; various childish pranks; play with a puppy; anger at His mother for letting the puppy go, and weeping; the puppy, in a divine body, singing of Hari, goes to Vaikuntha; seeing this, Brahma and others glorify Gaura; Gaurahari weeps when Shachi-devi is preparing the offering for the worship of Shashthi, and under cover of words reveals that He is the Lord of all.

**Chapter 3. Pauganda-lila (Boyhood)** (portions 14–17). Gaurasundara, having heard Murari Gupta expound the scripture of yoga, mocks him — Murari's anger; in return, to show the worthlessness of yoga and what the yogi comes to, [the Lord] urinates in Murari's plate during his midday meal and instructs him in the superiority of devotion to Krishna; the enactment of sankirtana with the boys of His age; in the conversation of Murari and Damodara — the sannyasa of Vishvarupa, the grief of Shachi and Jagannatha, and the pauganda-lila of Gaurasundara; the chudakarana and other rites for Gaurahari; at an auspicious hour, the beginning of writing; seeing that He is always absorbed in childish play and indifferent to study, Mishra Purandara punishes Him with reproaches and the like; at night in a dream Vishvambhara, revealing His divinity, admonishes the Mishra; the Mishra recognizes his son as Bhagavan, but on waking is again absorbed in parental love; Gaurahari's upanayana; the account of the avataras of the four yugas; in the Kali-yuga Krishna, accepting the bhava and lustre of Radha, descends in secret as Gaurasundara to preach the dharma of the age, the sankirtana of Hari's name, and, intoxicated with prema, gives prema away, offering it at the door of every living being; the instruction to His mother not to eat grain on the day of Ekadashi; when Mishra Jagannatha falls ill — instruction on the impermanence of human life and consolation of His mother; Shachi's grief after the Mishra's passing; Gaurahari's grief for His father and His zealous beginning of study.

**Chapter 4. Kaishora-lila (Youth) and the Wedding** (portions 18–19). Vanamali Acharya comes to Shachi-devi to propose a match for Gaurasundara; receiving no satisfactory answer from Shachi-devi, he returns with a heavy heart and on the way meets Gaura going home from His studies; by a hint [Gaura] gives His mother His consent to marry; at Shachi's call Vanamali Acharya comes and, going to the house of Vallabhacharya, arranges the marriage of Gaurahari with his daughter Lakshmi-devi; the announcement of the wedding and the invitation [of relatives]; on the day of adhivasa, according to family custom, anointing with turmeric and other [folk and Vedic] rites; Vallabha gives his daughter to Gaurahari, who arrives in great pomp with many companions, and after the feeding of brahmanas and the rest sends his daughter to his son-in-law's house.

**Chapter 5. Kaishora-lila and Banga-vijaya** (portions 20–21). Mahaprabhu goes with His friends to the bank of the Ganga; at the coming of her beloved Lord the Ganga rises [to] touch His feet; the story of a brahmana devoted to the Ganga; a Puranic legend: the Ganga arises from the feet of Sri Hari as He listened to the Devarshi singing of His qualities; Gaurahari, on the pretext of earning money, goes to East Bengal, shows mercy to the Padmavati and to the people of East Bengal and returns to Navadvipa; meanwhile Lakshmi-devi, bitten by the serpent of separation from the Lord, disappears from this world; when Shachi-devi, seeing the Lord, voices her grief, Gaurahari consoles His mother and tells the story of Lakshmi-devi.

**Chapter 6. The Second Wedding** (portions 21–22). Shachi-devi undertakes Vishvambhara's second marriage; through Kashinatha Mishra a match is arranged with the daughter of Sanatana Pandita; the customs and rites of the wedding; a splendid wedding, and the Mishra sends his daughter to his son-in-law's house.

**Chapter 7. The Journey to Gaya** (portion 23). Having completed the lila of study — the lila of teaching; on the pretext of offering pinda for His father — departure for Gaya; various pastimes on the way; under the pretext of fever — drinking water that has washed a brahmana's feet; [the teaching that] a man who does not worship Krishna is unfit to be a brahmana; the arrival in Gaya and darshana of the Vishnupada; the meeting with Ishvara Puri, the best of devotees, and the lila of receiving the mantra; having received the mantra, [the Lord] is intoxicated with prema for Krishna; at the sight of the Vishnupada — the rapture of prema and so on; then the return home.

## Madhya-khanda

**Chapter 1** (portions 24–25). The good fortune of [Mahaprabhu's] students [in His lila of teaching] is praised; [the Lord's] mercy to mother Shachi; the lila of the great manifestation of prema in the house of Shuklambara Brahmachari; the Lord's eight sattvika-bhavas during kirtana of Krishna; the Gaura-avatara of Sri Krishna, who accepted the bhava of a devotee, is the crown of all avataras; when the Lord performs the lila of preaching prema, the devotees headed by Gadadhara and devotees come from many regions and foreign lands gather together; by the Lord's mercy all go mad with prema; the Lord, maddened at the sound of Krishna's flute, hears a voice from heaven; in Murari's house [the Lord] shows the form of Varaha; Murari's hymn; Murari is told to worship the son of Nanda; at Murari's prayer the Lord shows the form of Sri Rama and glorifies the greatness of Krishna's name; the gods obtain prema; the Lord's mercy to Shuklambara, who sings the kirtana "O Radha, O Govinda"; the Lord gives Gadadhara the garland from His body; description of the beauty of Gaura and Gadadhara, [the inseparable] pair.

**Chapter 2** (portions 25–26). The author describes the beauty and charm of Mahaprabhu; the Lord plants a mango stone and distributes ripe fruit to the devotees; when [the tree] vanishes, [the Lord] shows the illusoriness of samsara and speaks of the way to conquer maya; to Mukunda Datta — instruction on the superiority of worshipping Sri Krishna and on serving Bhagavan, giving up speculation on the Self (*adhyatma*); blessing of Murari; the Lord delights in kirtana in Srivasa's house; when a foolish brahmana calls the form of Sri Krishna a product of maya, the Lord bathes in the Ganga in His clothes.

**Chapter 3** (portions 26–27). With kirtana the Lord goes to Advaita's house; the pre-eminence of bhakti alone in the age of Kali is glorified; a certain brahmana is bewildered; the Lord delights in kirtana in Advaita's house; returning home, the Lord expounds [the doctrine of] the Self by an example and glorifies the greatness of supreme bhakti; in Srivasa's house the Lord honours the club so as to destroy obstacles[^00-19]; Advaita Prabhu comes, [learning] of [the Lord's manifestation]; the greatness of Advaita is glorified; the Lord sits on the couch, and Advaita dances[^00-20]; the truth about Advaita is expounded, with instruction to serve Bhagavan.

**Chapter 4** (portion 28). Mahaprabhu explains [the meaning of the name] Srivasa; Murari [recites the *Raghuvira-ashtaka*], the Lord writes "Ramadasa" on his forehead and shows him the form of Rama; Sri Rama Pandita is told to serve his brother; devotees are sent to look for Nityananda Prabhu; the meeting with Nityananda in the house of Nandana Acharya; before everyone, the greatness of Nityananda is glorified and the way to attain prema for Krishna is described; Nityananda Prabhu is shown the six-armed, four-armed and two-armed forms.

**Chapter 5** (portion 29). The Lord weeps at night in the third watch and tells Shachi-devi His dream; Nityananda Prabhu stays two days with Advaita[^00-21]; Murari describes Nityananda Prabhu's manifestations of prema; Advaita worships Mahaprabhu in Srivasa's house; the meeting with Haridasa; Nityananda Prabhu takes leave of Mahaprabhu; the Lord distributes Nityananda Prabhu's kaupina, and the devotees tie it on their heads and dance; when Mahaprabhu disappears, the devotees suffer separation, and when He returns, their bliss increases.

**Chapter 6** (portions 30–31). Gaurahari plays with the devotees in the bliss of prema; [the devotees] accept the water from Nityananda Prabhu's feet; the meeting with Haridasa; Advaita Prabhu arrives; Advaita Prabhu is told to preach prema without distinguishing the worthy and the unworthy; the devotees are told [to go] from door to door distributing the name and prema; [when] the devotees mention the great sinners Jagai and Madhai, Mahaprabhu glorifies the greatness of the name, [which saves even] by namabhasa, and goes round the town with the devotees in kirtana; the deliverance of Jagai and Madhai; the author glorifies the mercy of Gaura and Nityananda.

**Chapter 7** (portions 31–32). The Lord's merciful glance on the brahmana Vanamali from East Bengal and his son; seeing [the Lord] in the form of Shyamasundara, they praise Him and call Him a "new Creator"; the Lord's bhava of Nrisimha in Srivasa's house; mercy to a worshipper of Shiva; when a brahmana woman touches the Lord's feet, He throws Himself into the Ganga; the Lord's instruction to serve Hari; mercy to Mukunda; Mukunda's hymn; the Lord manifests His form as Bhagavan; Srivasa performs [His] abhisheka; the author sings Gaura's qualities and instructs all to serve Gaura.

**Chapter 8** (portions 32–33)[^00-22]. [The Lord] makes a brahmana stricken with leprosy beg forgiveness of Srivasa and frees him from offence to a Vaishnava; when [the Lord] dances, the devotees do not admit a certain brahmana [to Him], and he curses Mahaprabhu: "May Your worldly happiness perish!"; the brahmana's hymn; the Lord consoles the brahmana; the Lord in the bhava of Balarama cries, "Give Me honey!"; He goes with the devotees to Advaita's house; in the bhava of Baladeva He faints; at Gadadhara's coming He restrains the bhava; the devotees headed by Acharyaratna arrive; all see [the Lord] in the form of Baladeva; bathing in the Ganga with the devotees.

**Chapter 9** (portions 33–34). The Lord in the bhava of Varaha; the devotees headed by Advaita Acharya are told [to perform] sankirtana and to preach it; in the bhava of a gopi He sings of the qualities of the gopis; [the Lord] goes to Chandrashekhara's house; Srivasa in the bhava of Narada; the greatness of Gadadhara is glorified: [the Lord] reveals that Gadadhara is Radhika; Thakura Haridasa arrives; the bliss of sankirtana; the Lord in the bhava of His majesty; in the bhava of Lakshmi He gives away the prema of service and at last is filled with the bhava of Ishvara.

**Chapter 10** (portions 35–36). The Lord, having told Srivasa of the dharmas of the four yugas, reveals the pre-eminence of sankirtana; in the bhava of Radha — agitation: "Where is Vrindavana? Where is Lalita?"; at Murari's words [the Lord] calms down and delights in kirtana; He tells mother Shachi that in a dream He received the mantra of sannyasa; the arrival of Keshava Bharati; the Lord's separation from Krishna [becomes] unbearable; at the thought that the Lord will take sannyasa the devotees lament bitterly, and the Lord consoles them.

**Chapter 11** (portions 36–37). Hearing that the Lord will take sannyasa, mother Shachi grieves and asks [Him] to keep the dharma [of a householder]; the Lord, telling the story of Dhruva, instructs her to serve Krishna; having consoled His mother in various ways, He shows her the form of Sri Krishna and dispels her grief.

**Chapter 12** (portion 38)[^00-23]. The grief of Vishnupriya-devi; the Lord consoles her with many sweet words and, having instructed her in the truth, shows the four-armed form of Narayana; Srivasa, Murari and other devotees arrive; the Lord consoles them.

**Chapter 13** (portions 39–40). Having consoled the devotees with instruction in the truth, [the Lord,] in order to take sannyasa, crosses the Ganga and comes to Kantakanagara, to Keshava Bharati; Nityananda Prabhu consoles mother Shachi and Vishnupriya; with Chandrashekhara Acharya, Damodara Pandita and other devotees Nityananda Prabhu comes to Kantakanagara; [the Lord] asks Bharati for the mantra of sannyasa; Bharati refuses, and then, [knowing the Lord's heart], agrees to give the mantra; the Lord speaks the mantra of sannyasa into Bharati's ear, and Bharati gives [Him] the mantra; the grief of the villagers at the Lord's sannyasa; the Lord consoles them and, [behaving] as a devotee, asks [for the blessing of] devotion to Krishna; the Lord's sannyasa name is Sri Krishna Chaitanya; having taken sannyasa, He wanders through Rarh intoxicated with prema.

**Chapter 14** (portion 41). When Chandrashekhara Acharya returns from Kantakanagara to Nadia — the grief and lament of mother Shachi and Vishnupriya; Nityananda Prabhu announces Mahaprabhu's coming to Shantipura; conversation of mother Shachi with Nityananda Prabhu; the people of Nadia come to Advaita's house to see the Lord; Mahaprabhu speaks fittingly with everyone.

**Chapter 15** (portions 42–43). The Lord instructs the devotees to benefit all living beings by the sankirtana of Hari's name; as He prepares to go to Nilachala, Thakura Haridasa speaks humbly with the Lord; when the devotees follow, [the Lord] consoles them with sweet words and sets out for Nilachala reciting the verse "Rama Raghava, Rama Raghava, protect me" and others; on the way Nityananda Prabhu breaks the staff, and the Lord shows a lila of anger.

**Chapter 16** (portions 44–45). On the way to Nilachala — through Tamoluk; bathing in the Brahma-kunda; having seen Madhusudana, [the Lord] comes to Remuna and dances and sings before Gopaladeva; having bathed in the Vaitarani, He sees Lord Varaha; He comes to Yajapura and, having seen the lingas of Shiva, sees Viraja; from there — bathing in the Brahma-kunda, Nabhi-gaya and the city of Shiva; a merciful glance on the toll-collector, and the form of the One who lies on the Milk Ocean shown [to him] in a dream; arrival at Ekamra-kanana, hymns to Shiva and a discussion of whether to accept Shiva's prasada; via Kapoteshvara to the river Bhargavi and bathing in it; [at the sight of] the temple of Sri Jagannatha — swoon; [the Lord is carried] to the house of Vasudeva Sarvabhauma and [brought to His senses]; standing behind the Garuda pillar, the Lord sees Jagannatha; discussion with Sarvabhauma Pandita on various subjects and the showing [to him] of the six-armed form.

## Shesha-khanda

**Chapter 1** (portions 46–47). Kirtana with Sarvabhauma in Puri; [the Lord] sets out on the road to Setubandha; He comes to Kurma-kshetra and delivers Vasudeva; He comes to Jiyada-Nrisimha — an ancient legend is told; meeting with Ramananda in the city of Kanchi[^00-25] and the showing of the form of Rasaraja-Mahabhava; through Panchavati to Shrirangam; mercy to Trimalla Bhatta; there [the Lord] spends the chaturmasya; meeting with Paramananda Puri; Puri praises Bhagavan Gaura.

**Chapter 2** (portions 47–50). On the way to Setubandha — the deliverance of the seven palms and the ancient story of the seven palms; at Setubandha, intoxicated with prema, [the Lord] utters the names of Rama, Lakshmana, Sita, Hanuman and others; from the Godavari — return to Alalanatha; [the Lord] accepts Vishnudasa as His own; He comes again to Purushottama; sets out to see Mathura-mandala; meeting with Rupa and Sanatana; with Krishnadasa He sees both banks of the Yamuna, the twelve forests and other places of Krishna's lila.

**Chapter 3** (portions 51–52). Krishnadasa humbly entreats at the Lord's feet; the Lord takes the road to Nilachala; on the way He drinks buttermilk from a cowherd, and the cowherd obtains the Lord's mercy; the Lord comes to Gauda, through Rarh to Kuliya; people come from Navadvipa to see the Lord; Shachi sees her son and weeps piteously; at His mother's wish the Lord comes to Navadvipa and enlightens His mother to serve Krishna; the Lord comes to Shantipura, to Advaita's house, and there sings the name; the Lord leaves Shantipura and goes via Tamoluk to Nilachala; darshana of Jagannatha and kirtana day and night; King Prataparudra obtains the Lord's mercy and sees the six-armed form; a poor brahmana from Dravida, tormented by poverty, comes to Nilachala, fasts for seven days, meets Vibhishana and at last obtains the Lord's mercy[^00-24].

[^00-1]: The title page of the edition reads: *Sri Sri Chaitanya-mangala*, composed by the venerable Lochana Dasa Thakura; edited by Sri Srimad Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Gosvami (with his titles: "paramahamsa-parivrajakacharya, best of the Rupanugas, sole protector of the Sri Brahma-Madhva-Gaudiya sampradaya"), "with preface, various indices and so on"; published by Sri Chaitanya Math in ancient Sri Dhama Mayapura, Bengali year 1329 (1922). Besides the text of the poem, the edition contains a guru-parampara in verse, the preface, indices of quoted verses and scriptures, a synopsis of contents by parts and chapters (*Khanda-vivarana*), an index of words and prose summaries before the sections. We used the OCR text of the scan (Digital Library of India).
[^00-2]: In the 1903 edition this chapter is given not in the main text but in a footnote (see en/52, footnote 8). The editor of 1903, Atulakrishna Gosvami, says in his foreword that he used four complete and three incomplete manuscripts and three printed editions; lines not found in all manuscripts he puts in brackets. The 1983 edition (Haridasa Shastri) divides the text into chapters differently from Bhaktisiddhanta (see e.g. en/43, footnote 1); we follow Bhaktisiddhanta's division and numbering.
[^00-3]: Before the preface the edition has the title page and a guru-parampara in verse (the succession of teachers from Krishna and Brahma through Madhva, Madhavendra Puri and Ishvara Puri to Sri Chaitanya and onward, down to Bhaktivinoda Thakura and Gaurakishora Dasa Babaji); after the preface, an alphabetical index of the Sanskrit verses quoted in the poem and a list of the scriptures quoted. These we do not translate. The OCR text of the preface is damaged in places: what is restored by sense is given in square brackets, what is unreadable is marked […]. The bold subheadings are in the edition.
[^00-4]: E.g. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 8.34: "Vrindavana Dasa wrote the *Chaitanya-mangala*."
[^00-5]: Jayananda is the author of a *Chaitanya-mangala*, a life of the Lord found in manuscripts and first published at the beginning of the twentieth century. Bhaktisiddhanta regards this book as unreliable and contrary to siddhanta; among other things it tells of the Lord's departure from a wound in the foot (cf. en/52, footnote 10). We render his judgement as it stands.
[^00-6]: *A-chaitanya-mangala* is a play on words: "not-Chaitanya-mangala", a book alien to Chaitanya and bringing no blessing.
[^00-7]: The *Chaitanya-charita-mahakavya* is a Sanskrit poem by Kavi-karnapura. The *Gaura-krishnodaya* is a Sanskrit poem by Govinda, a poet of Utkala (Orissa). *Sri Chaitanya-mangala* here is apparently Lochana Dasa's poem itself.
[^00-8]: The OCR reads «তমাল»; probably «ভক্তমাল», the *Bhaktamala* of Laladasa. The words that follow are unreadable. The *Baul-chandrika* is apparently a work of the Baul community, one of those Bhaktisiddhanta counts as deviant (*apasampradayas*).
[^00-9]: *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 23.39: "The words, actions and gestures of one in whose heart prema for Krishna has arisen are not understood even by the wise."
[^00-10]: Gaura-nagari (gaura-nagari-vada) is the doctrine that worships Gaura as a *nagara*, the lover of women, after the pattern of Krishna and the gopis; the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Vrindavana Dasa and Krishnadasa Kaviraja, reject it. On Lochana Dasa's own lines in this vein see en/09, footnote 9, and en/18, footnotes 5–6.
[^00-11]: According to the list well known in the Gaudiya Math, the thirteen apasampradayas are: aul, baul, kartabhaja, nera, darvesh, sani, sahajiya, sakhibheki, smarta, jat-gosani, ativadi, chudadhari and gauranga-nagari.
[^00-12]: Sutra-khanda, vv. 53–56 (en/01); the translation is the same as there.
[^00-13]: The OCR reads «পচপ্রকার»; we read «পঞ্চপ্রকার», "of five kinds". Bhaktisiddhanta does not explain which five metres are meant.
[^00-14]: We read «স্বপ্নমূলে» ("on the basis of dreams"; the OCR has «স্বপ্রমূলে»); the reading is not entirely certain.
[^00-15]: The tradition is usually told thus: Vrindavana Dasa's book was at first called *Chaitanya-mangala* and was renamed *Chaitanya-bhagavata* when Lochana Dasa's poem of the same name appeared; Vrindavana Dasa's mother, Narayani, figures in the story. Bhaktisiddhanta's text is damaged here, and the sense is given according to the most probable reading. In scholarly literature Lochana Dasa's poem is usually dated later than the *Chaitanya-bhagavata*.
[^00-16]: Gunaraja Khan (Maladhara Basu) is the author of the *Sri Krishna-vijaya* (1470s), a Bengali retelling of the *Bhagavata*; the Lord praised his book (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 15.99–100).
[^00-17]: In the edition the *Khanda-vivarana* stands after the indices, before the index of words; the pages of the edition are given for each chapter, and these we omit. The OCR text is damaged in places; gaps are filled by sense and from Bhaktisiddhanta's summaries before the sections (translated at the head of the corresponding sections), with which this synopsis agrees in content. The division into chapters is Bhaktisiddhanta's: the Sutra-khanda has two sections, the Adi-khanda 7 chapters, the Madhya-khanda 16, the Shesha-khanda 3. The section headings in our translation stand where a section begins in the text and do not always coincide with this division.
[^00-18]: So in Lochana Dasa (Sutra-khanda, en/08, footnote 8). According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 6), Advaita is Maha-Vishnu, one with Sada-Shiva.
[^00-19]: Cf. en/27, v. 64: "I have honoured the club in order to destroy these villains."
[^00-20]: Cf. en/27, v. 78. Seated on the couch (*khatta*), the Lord accepts worship as Bhagavan.
[^00-21]: The OCR text is damaged here; restored from Bhaktisiddhanta's summary for this chapter (en/29).
[^00-22]: In our translation this chapter also contains the section "Shachi's grief over the brahmana's curse" (en/33), which is absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition; it is translated from the 1983 edition.
[^00-23]: In our translation this chapter also contains the section "Consoling the devotees" (en/38), which is absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition; it is translated from the 1983 edition.
[^00-24]: Bhaktisiddhanta's text ends with the story of the brahmana from Dravida (in the edition a separate section with its own numbering; in our translation "The Last Pastimes", en/52). The chapter on the Lord's disappearance and Lochana Dasa's closing words are translated from the 1983 edition (en/52).
[^00-25]: So in Lochana Dasa (en/46). According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 8), the Lord met Ramananda Raya on the bank of the Godavari.

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 1 (portion 01)

*The blessing: the mangala-shloka; praise of Ganesha, of Shiva and Parvati, of Sarasvati and of the Vaishnavas; obeisance to the Lord, His associates and the author's guru Narahari; Murari Gupta and his Sanskrit book, the model of this poem; the beginning of the "sutra", a brief list of the pastimes of the Adi-khanda*

## Mangalacharana (The Blessing)

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.*[^01-1] Having sung the glory of Sri Krishna Chaitanya, the author prays for the merciful grace first of the Vaishnavas and then of his own guru, Sri Narahari Thakura; he bows at the feet of the countless associates of Gaura-lila, known to him and unknown; praying for the blessing of guru and Vaishnavas, he declares that the model of his book, a *panchali-prabandha*, is the Sanskrit poem of Sri Murari Gupta called *Sri Chaitanya-charita*; and he briefly states what will be written in the Adi-, Madhya- and Shesha-khanda.

*Mangala-shloka (Sanskrit)*[^01-2]

Glory to the Lord in the form of Chaitanya, crest-jewel of sannyasis! —
He who gladdens the devotees by giving away heaps of priceless jewels of bhakti and prema,
who descended in full in the age of Kali to free the devotees from their distress,
and who crushes the unbelievers of the three worlds with the thunderbolt-goad of His roar.

*Raga patha-manjari*

**1.** I bow, I bow, I praise you, god Ganeshvara,
great destroyer of obstacles;
one-tusked, huge of body, helper in every work —
glory, glory to the son of Parvati![^01-3]

**2.** I bow my head to Hara and Gauri, joining my palms;
falling at their feet, I serve them:
the sole doers in the three worlds, granting the boon of bhakti to Vishnu —
these two are to me all the gods and goddesses.

**3.** I bow my head to Sarasvati: play upon my tongue,
tell the tale of the virtues of Gaurahari —
unknown to the three worlds, unknown even to the Lord of speech in all his greatness[^01-4] —
a wondrous, unprecedented tale.

**4.** Humbly I beseech the gods and all my venerable elders:
let no one put obstacles here.
I ask neither wealth nor boons — I am the lowest of the low:
let the book be completed without hindrance.

**5.** First of all I praise the devotees of Vishnu, all those great fortunate ones
by whose virtues the earth is made pure:
they are merciful to all living beings, and above all to those in whom they find longing [for God];
their lives are a blessing to the three worlds.

**6.** I am quite unworthy, I do not know right from left,
and yet I want to seize the sky with my arms;
a blind man choosing heavenly gems, who does not see even the mountain beside him —
I do not know what the end will be.

**7.** I have but one hope: the Lord rejects no one —
high and low alike sing His glories;
to all living beings He is equally merciful, to all He gives the shade of His feet,
He keeps no rule of who is worthy.

**8.** And the Vaishnavas — hear of them:
they are causelessly merciful to all people,
they live for others, adorn themselves for others,
and find their happiness in the good of others.

**9.** Thakura Sri Narahari, master of his servant's life —
my hope is in his feet;
low though I am, I long to sing the virtues of Gaura —
this is what Lochana Dasa trusts in.

**10.** By the grace of his feet I shall sing without weariness —
this is the hope of my heart;
those two feet, the cause of attaining what I desire,
I keep in my heart unceasingly.

*Raga kedara*

**11.** Glory, glory to Sri Krishna Chaitanya and Nityananda,
glory to Advaitachandra, glory to the devotees of Gaura!

**12.** Glory to the Lord of the life of Narahari and Gadadhara!
Be merciful, Lord, cast Your auspicious glance on me.

**13.** His golden, fair body is all adorned with compassion;
bowing down, I shall sing of those cool, rosy feet.

**14.** With all the devotees, be pleased to sit [here] in love:
let the breeze of those cool feet touch my body.

**15.** Darling of Shachi, Lord, I bow to You:
for even a moment turn Your merciful glance on me.

**16.** Advaita Acharya Gosani, crest-jewel of the gods —
by the grace of His feet this earth is blessed.

**17.** Bowing down, I shall sing of Him, the Lord of Sita's life:
be merciful, Lord — I pray with joined palms.

**18.** He is not different from Chaitanya — that Thakura, the Avadhuta,
Nityananda-Rama; I praise the son of Rohini.

**19.** Garga, enraptured with pride in the virtues of Gora[^01-5] —
bowing down, I first sing of his feet.

**20.** I praise Mishra Purandara, father of Vishvambhara;
I praise Shachi Thakurani, mother of the Thakura.

**21.** I praise Lakshmi Thakurani, renowned in the world —
the serpent of separation from the Lord stung her.[^01-6]

**22.** I praise Vishnupriya-ma, [the lady] of Navadvipa:
her ornament is the rosy feet of the Lord.

**23.** Joyfully I praise Pundarika Vidyanidhi,
for whom Mahaprabhu wept aloud.[^01-7]

**24.** With one mind I praise Pandita Gosani[^01-8];
I bow at the feet of Ishvara Puri and Madhava Puri.

**25.** I praise Govinda Gosani and Vakreshvara —
intoxicated bees at the lotus feet of Gaura.

**26.** Puri Paramananda and Vishnupuri,
and Gadadhara Dasa I praise — [their feet] upon my head.

**27.** Gupta the vaidya[^01-9] I praise with joyful longing:
be kind at heart, that I may sing the virtues of Gora.

**28.** I praise Srivasa Thakura and Haridasa;
in the feet of Vasu Datta and Mukunda is my hope.

**29.** I praise Ramananda Raya, the abode of love;
unceasingly I praise Pandita Jagadananda.

**30.** I praise Rupa and Sanatana, Pandita Damodara;
I praise Raghava Pandita, bowing without end.

**31.** Sri Rama, Sundara, Gauridasa and all the rest,
the companions of Nityananda, all the devotees — I praise.

**32.** I praise the master of our family, my chosen object of worship:[^01-10]
in this world and the next he is my protector.

**33.** Without him I have no friend in the three worlds;
I praise Narahari Dasa, ocean of Gaura's virtues.

**34.** To Govinda and Madhava Ghosha and to Vasu Ghosha,
falling to the ground, with joined palms, I bow.

**35.** With one mind I praise Sri Vrindavana Dasa,
by whose song, the *Bhagavata*,[^01-11] the world is enchanted.

**36.** Brothers, one could sing praises without end;
I praise the Deity of our house and Raghunandana.[^01-12]

**36+1.** Sri Raghunandana, beloved of all the mahantas —
him to whom the Lord, before all others, gave a garland and sandal.[^01-13]

**37.** As a child he fed laddus to the Sri Murti[^01-14] —
no one took him for an ordinary man.

**38.** I praise his father, Sri Mukunda Dasa,
whose faith is pure on the path approved by Chaitanya.

**39.** Some names I know, some I do not;
I praise them all: all of them are the crown on my head.

**40.** I praise the mahantas and the people of the mahantas;
in one place I praise and sing the feet of all.

**41.** Let no one judge in his mind who comes first and who after:
for the sake of the metre the praises are not in order.

**42.** Those whom by mistake I have not named in my praise —
to them a hundred obeisances: forgive me the offence.

**43.** I praise the devotees who live on earth and who roam the sky;
I bow at the feet of each, one by one.

**44.** Sing the virtues of Gora with joy, in hope of love —
so sings Lochana Dasa with a jubilant heart.[^01-15]

*Raga varadi; disha: "Brothers dear as life, I will tell, I will tell you my own tale! — Murchha: — Ah, what is this, ah, O my life! — First I beg the blessing of all the great fortunate ones; then shall I sing the tale of [Gaura's] virtues"*

**45.** I am worthless, lowest of the low — what do I know of the truth?
What can I say of the greatness of Gora's virtues and deeds?

**46.** Not knowing, to babble on — what good is that?
To stumble before worthy people is a shame.

**47.** I am not qualified, and yet I dare:
so strongly am I drawn by the sweetness of Gora's virtues.

**48.** Murari Gupta, the vaidya, lives in Navadvipa;
he is always near Gorachand.

**49.** Who could describe his greatness?
On earth he is renowned as Hanuman[^01-16] —

**50.** he who leapt across the ocean and burned Lanka,
found out the news of Sita and told it to Sri Rama,

**51.** who brought the vishalya-karani herb and revived Lakshmana —
that same Murari Gupta lives in Nadia.

**52.** He knows all the truth; he is the Lord's intimate,
a seasoned devotee of Gaura's lotus feet.

**53.** All that the Lord did from His birth in childhood,
how from beginning to end He spread prema —

**54.** about all this Damodara Pandita questioned him,
and he told him everything from beginning to end in due order.

**55.** So came about a book of Gauranga's deeds composed in verse[^01-17] —
the dialogue with Damodara, issued from Murari's mouth.

**56.** Hearing it, I was filled with love,
and I tell of Gaura's deeds in a sung narrative, a *panchali*.

**57.** I am not qualified, and yet I tell it — that is my fault;
do not scorn me, do not be angry.

**58.** Who, seeing nectar, is not drawn to it?
A foolish child wants [to reach] the moon in the sky.

**59.** Such is my wish — to sing the virtues of Gora;
at this hour I beg the mercy of the Vaishnavas.

**60.** I bow at the feet of the Vaishnavas:
to sing the virtues of Gora — that is my heart's desire.

**61.** My Thakura, Prabhu Narahari Dasa —
I bow and humbly pray: fulfil my desire.

*Raga mara-hati; disha: "Hari, Rama, Rama, Moon among brahmanas — ah, my life!"*

**62.** First of all I will tell an unprecedented tale:
how Acharya Gosani[^01-18] worshipped [the Lord in] the womb.

**63.** The Lord of the three worlds came to earth,
with all His companions, intimates and retinue;

**64.** how father and mother raised the child,
how at the annaprashana they joyfully gave Him a name.

**65.** I shall tell in due order the pastimes of childhood:
[how] the jingle of ankle-bells was heard from bare feet;

**66.** how He suddenly went about touching unclean places,
and how He instructed His mother in knowledge;

**67.** the women of the town spoke, to understand His ways,
and at their word He at once brought a coconut;

**68.** the Thakura plays with a puppy —
seeing it, everyone is full of joy;

**69.** He plays with the children on the royal road —
and how Gupta the vaidya beheld His revelation;

**70.** with the boys He dances in the sankirtana of Hari —
seeing it, everyone rejoices at heart;

**71.** how His father put the chalk [for writing] in His hand —
hearing of it, the fever of misfortune goes far away;

**72.** then I shall tell — listen attentively —
how Vishvambhara plays with the elder, Vishvarupa:

**73.** like two brothers born of one womb, Indra and Upendra;
I shall tell of them — you will hear later;

**74.** how Vishvarupa took sannyasa,
and how Vishvambhara consoled father and mother with words;

**75.** then I shall tell of Vishvambhara's deeds:
how He played wild games with the children —

**76.** all the boys together on the bank of the Jahnavi
run over the sand, following the tracks of birds;

**77.** seeing this, His father was grieved at heart,
brought Him home and scolded Him;

**78.** how [the Lord] showed him mercy in a dream —
I shall tell it all, listen with one mind;

**79.** the ear-piercing, the chudakarana and the sacred thread —
I shall tell it all with a joyful heart;

**80.** childhood ended, He enters youth,
day by day manifesting prema without end;

**81.** He studied with His guru together with His fellow students,
how He mimicked the speech of the people of East Bengal;[^01-19]

[^01-1]: The summary printed in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition before this section is almost wholly illegible in the OCR (only scattered words can be made out — a list of the coming pastimes: Vishvarupa's sannyasa, the passing of Jagannatha Mishra, the journey to East Bengal, etc.). We therefore give his own brief summary of this section from the *Khanda-vivarana* (survey of the parts) at the beginning of the same edition.
[^01-2]: In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition the shloka is followed by his Sanskrit commentary. He explains: "in the age of Kali" — "in the age of quarrel"; "to free the devotees from their distress" — to give the Lord's servants a joy free of thorns when they are troubled by disputants opposed to shruti; "descended in full" — appeared in His original form as Bhagavan (svayam-rupa); "crest-jewel of sannyasis" — the jewel in the crown of renunciants, full of beauty; "bhakti and prema" — service of the Lord, and prema, which is nothing but the desire to please Krishna's senses — these are the priceless jewels, and "giving away" means bestowing them freely; "the devotees" — those devoted to pure service; "the unbelievers" — those hostile to the devotees and turned away from Hari; "crushes" — subdues completely; "glory to" — may He abide above all.
[^01-3]: Ganesha, Shiva with Parvati, and Sarasvati are praised here after the custom of the Bengali *mangala* poems. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the gods are honoured as devotees of the Lord: Ganesha worships Govinda (*Brahma-samhita* 5.50), and Shiva is the greatest of Vaishnavas (*Bhag.* 12.13.16); so too Bhaktisiddhanta, in his summary of the next section, calls Shambhu "the best of Vaishnavas". The words "the sole doers in the three worlds" are poetic praise; for the tradition the source of all the worlds is Krishna Himself, and Shiva and Parvati are His servants, "granting bhakti to Vishnu", as the same line says.
[^01-4]: Literally "unknown to the three worlds, in greatness — to the Lord of speech"; the Lord of speech (Vaninatha) is Brahma, husband of Sarasvati. The 1903 edition reads "to the golden [Lord], the Lord of speech"; the sense of the line is not quite clear in either reading.
[^01-5]: So in Bhaktisiddhanta's text (and in his index of names — "Garga"): Garga Muni, the family priest of the Yadavas, who in Gaura-lila is Nilambara Chakravarti, the Lord's maternal grandfather (*Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*). The 1983 edition reads here "brimming over, enraptured with pride in Gora's virtues" and refers the line to Nityananda.
[^01-6]: The Lord's first wife, Lakshmipriya, left the world while the Lord was in East Bengal (see en/02, v. 86).
[^01-7]: In the 1983 edition this couplet stands right after the praise of Shachi (v. 20); we follow the order of Bhaktisiddhanta's edition.
[^01-8]: Pandita Gosani is Gadadhara Pandita.
[^01-9]: That is, Murari Gupta the physician (*vaidya*, physician, also the physician caste).
[^01-10]: "Master of the family" and "chosen Deity" (*ishta-devata*) here mean Narahari himself, the author's guru, named in the next couplet.
[^01-11]: Vrindavana Dasa's *Sri Chaitanya-bhagavata*.
[^01-12]: Literally "the Thakura of our house"; this usually means the household Deity (at Shrikhanda, Gopinatha). The 1983 edition reads: "I praise the Thakura of our house, Sri Raghunandana", i.e. Raghunandana himself is so called.
[^01-13]: V. 36+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; it is absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition and from the 1903 edition. On Raghunandana and his father Mukunda Dasa cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 15.112–131.
[^01-14]: Sri Murti — the Deity. According to the tradition of Shrikhanda, the boy Raghunandana, son of Mukunda Dasa (Narahari's brother), gave a laddu to the Deity of Gopinatha, and the Deity ate it.
[^01-15]: The line is damaged in the OCR of Bhaktisiddhanta's edition; translated from the legible reading. The 1903 and 1983 editions have: "Sing the virtues of Gora — this is my hope; Lochana Dasa says: fulfil my desire."
[^01-16]: The identification of Murari Gupta with Hanuman is also accepted by Kavi Karnapura's *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*.
[^01-17]: Murari Gupta's *Sri Krishna-chaitanya-charitamrita*, a Sanskrit poem framed as Murari's dialogue with Damodara Pandita (translated in `murari-gupta/`). Bhaktisiddhanta in his survey of the parts calls it *Sri Chaitanya-charita*.
[^01-18]: Advaita Acharya.
[^01-19]: The people of East Bengal (*bangaja*) spoke a dialect that was thought comic in Navadvipa; in His youth the Lord loved to mimic it.

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 2 (portion 02)

*The end of the "sutra": a list of the pastimes of the Adi- and Madhya-khanda; praise of Chaitanya, Nityananda and Advaita; the narrative begins — Damodara's question about the reasons for Gauranga's appearance, Murari's answer on the decline of dharma in Kali, and Narada's grief*

**82.** how He bade His mother keep Ekadashi —
of His many revelations I shall tell in due time;

**83.** how at that time Jagannatha [Mishra] passed to the other world,
and how the Lord wept, grieving for His father.

**84.** Then I shall tell another wondrous tale:
the Lord married — joy without bounds;

**85.** and the secret that came about when He saw the Ganga[^02-1] —
listen attentively, I shall surely tell it;

**86.** I shall tell well of the journey to East Bengal
and of how Lakshmi ascended to heaven;

**87.** returning to His own land He married again,
imparted knowledge to His disciples and set out for Gaya.

**88.** Of each I shall tell — listen, all of you:
you will find great joy; do not give up your effort.

**89.** Especially I shall tell of the return home:
He constantly manifests prema, absorbed in rasa.

**90.** The tale of the Madhya-khanda, brothers, is a great joy:
hearing it the hair stands on end — nectar without end.

**91.** The tale of the meeting with the devotees is the manifestation of prema:
even before I begin to tell it, my heart rejoices.

**92.** The tale of the Madhya-khanda, brothers, is the play in Nadia:
like a stream of nectar — so prema was spread.

**93.** The Lord revealed most unprecedented pastimes,
such as devotees never heard of in any of the four yugas.

**94.** This wondrous tale, the spreading of bhakti,
I shall tell in the Madhya-khanda — the play in Nadia.

**95.** How all the devotees came together —
of each I shall tell, as far as I know how to tell.

**96.** First I shall tell how Shachi received the gift of prema,
how on the road He heard the sound of the flute,

**97.** how, seized by bhava, He was overwhelmed with prema,
and suddenly a voice from heaven sounded in the sky;

**98.** how in the form of Varaha He showed mercy to Murari;
Brahma and the other gods behold [it], themselves enraptured;

**99.** how Shuklambara Brahmachari then obtained prema —
I shall tell it all, listen with all your heart;

**100.** Pandita Sri Gadadhara, [dear to] the Lord as life,[^02-2]
overwhelmed with prema, weeps day and night;

**101.** one by one He gave prema to all —
I shall tell it all as is fitting;

**102.** His mercy to a devotee — how He planted a mango stone:
hearing of it, all will be freed from doubt;

**103.** the Lord, eclipsing the doctrine of the Self (*adhyatma*), reveals prema:
He is not reached by knowledge — so He explains to all.

**104.** Then I shall tell an unprecedented account —
how the meeting with Nityananda came about,

**105.** how Haridasa meets the Lord,
of the meeting of Advaita Acharya with Nityananda,

**106.** how He delivered Jagai and Madhai,
how He showed mercy to a father and son, brahmanas,

**107.** how He showed mercy to a singer [of songs] of Shiva;
how suddenly grief arose from a brahmana's deed,[^02-3]

**108.** how the Lord threw Himself into the Jahnavi —
hearing of it, hearts will tremble in the three worlds;

**109.** then you will hear in due order another wonder —
how the Lord cleansed the temple;

**110.** you will hear many quite unprecedented things:
He delivered a leper — a great marvel;

**111.** especially I shall tell how the bhava of Balarama took hold of Him —
hearing of it, all will find joy without end;

**112.** the revelation in the house of Sri Chandrashekhara Acharya:
the radiance of prema floods earth and sky;

**113.** many secrets I shall tell there —
how wondrous renunciation awoke in the Lord,

**114.** how, seeing Keshava Bharati in the town of Nadia,
He rejoices at heart: "I will take sannyasa!";

**115.** how all the devotees lamented,
how Shachi and Vishnupriya plunged into an ocean of grief;

**116.** in hope of sannyasa He leaves Navadvipa
and takes sannyasa with Bharati's help.

**117.** I shall tell properly all the details:
how He went to the house of Acharya Prabhu,[^02-4]

**118.** what was said at the meeting with everyone,
how, consoling all, the Lord set out on His way,

**119.** how He went to see Purushottama;
I shall tell the secret of the village of Remuna;

**120.** little by little I shall tell of the events on the road —
hearing of them, all people will be filled with love;

**121.** what wonder the Lord worked on coming to Jajpur —
I shall surely tell of the town of Ekamra too;

**122.** how the meeting with Jagannatha came about,
the revelation to Sarvabhauma — you will hear; listen with one mind.

**123.** The tale of the Madhya-khanda, brothers, is the essence of nectar;
the tale of the Shesha-khanda remains — I shall tell it; hear further.

**124.** The Madhya-khanda in this book[^02-5] is the manifestation of prema —
so says Lochana Dasa with a jubilant heart.

*Raga dhanashi; taraja metre*

**125.** Glory, glory, glory to Sri Krishna Chaitanya —
He Himself has descended to earth!
Ah, what fortune for the people! The earth is full of pride:
her ornament is His holy feet.

**126.** The lamp of the three worlds has risen in Navadvipa,
shining with rays of compassion;
the devotees who had thirsted for many days
have obtained [what they hoped for] — the hope of prema.

**127.** Like six-footed bees circling a honeyed lotus,
like the meeting of moon and chakora,
like the chataka bird that, seeing a rain-cloud,
cries out in rapture: "Piu, piu!"

**128.** The enraptured devotees dance, Gora rains down prema,
hunkaras thunder, roars, lion-like cries;
like a poor man who lost his wealth and has found it again —
so His followers weep in their longing.

**129.** Like a forest elephant caught in a forest fire
that plunges into an ocean of nectar,
so in the play of prema they were immersed entirely
and forgot the former burning.

**130.** "Bravo, Thakura!" they cry; someone slaps his arms;
in the bliss of prema they forget themselves.
The prema that Lakshmi begs for with joined palms and ardent love,
He gives away to all without discrimination.

**131.** What more can I say? Even Ananta forgot where He was —
what rasa, what sweetness of prema!
He who is called Shesha, who holds the world upon His head,
now bears the name Nitai.

**132.** Brimming with the rasa of prema, knowing no difference between own and other,
He explains this to all;
under the measured tread of His feet the earth sways,
as when a maddened elephant in rut goes by.

**133.** Hear of another wonder: Mahesha, named Advaita,[^02-6]
whose virtues, when sung, surpass understanding,
in His talks with Thakura Chaitanya on the rasa of prema
forgot His knowledge and His yoga.

**134.** With their rasika companions, in the sport of love's pastimes,
They explain everything to all without dispute;
apart from these two Thakuras there is no Thakura of mercy —
it is for Their sake that Gorachand rose.

**135.** "Glory, glory!" — auspicious cries ring out, the people of the world say "Hari!",
all are hoping for prema;
prema, rare even for Brahma — all long for it —
so says Lochana Dasa, smiling.

## Grantharambha (The Narrative Begins)

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* At the beginning of the narrative the author, relying on the conversation of Narada and Uddhava from the *Jaimini-bharata*, which is related in the dialogue of Damodara and Murari, has set down the reason why Krishna descended in the form of Gaura.
>
> Once the devarshi Narada, grieved by the sufferings of the living beings stricken by Kali and pondering how to save them, resolved to move the protector of dharma, Bhagavan Sri Krishna, to descend into the mortal world, and set out for Dvaraka. Sri Krishna was then staying in the palace of Rukmini-devi. Rukmini-devi, having learned that Krishna, accepting the bhava of Radha, would descend in the form of Gaura, was in the greatest distress at the thought of the coming separation; she was glorifying His lotus feet and confiding her grief to Him when the devarshi Narada arrived. Received by Krishna with honour, he told the reason for his coming, and Krishna revealed to him that He would descend in the form of Gaura in the house of Shachi and Jagannatha at Navadvipa, and at once showed him His form as Gaura.
>
> Seeing the supremely lovely form of Gaura, the devarshi Narada, beside himself with rapture, went on from there meditating on the form of Gaura and singing the glory of Gaura, the essence of all avataras, and came to Naimisharanya. There the best of devotees, Uddhava, asked the great sage how the living beings stricken by Kali could be saved; the devarshi Narada told him all that had happened, sang the glory of the Kali-yuga, the essence of all yugas, and of the dharma of that age, the sankirtana of Hari-nama, and came to Kailasa, to the best of Vaishnavas, Shambhu. There Narada, wishing to remind Parvati of her former vow, [related]: how, learning of the greatness of mahaprasada, he served Lakshmi for twelve years to obtain it, and by her grace obtained mahaprasada and gave a portion to Shiva; how Shiva, having tasted the mahaprasada, broke into a wild dance; how the Earth, unable to bear Shiva's dance, came to Parvati; and then the greatness of mahaprasada and Parvati's vow to distribute mahaprasada to all living beings; and he sang much of the descent of Gaura.
>
> Then Narada came to Brahma and told of Gaurasundara's descent in the Kali-yuga. Brahma quoted and explained to Narada the verses of the *Srimad-Bhagavata* concerning this descent, supported the sense of these verses of the *Bhagavata* with the words of other scriptures, and said that at the time of Gaura's descent he himself would appear on earth with all the gods. Next comes the proposal of the gods to take birth in the mortal world; [the account of how] Krishna, accepting the bhava and lustre of Srimati Radhika, together with the special qualities of His eternal consorts Rukmini, Satyabhama and others, taking up the weapon of the sankirtana of the Name, descends as Gaura, Balarama as Nityananda, Shiva as Advaita Prabhu, and the other associates as Murari, Mukunda, Srinivasa, Raya Ramananda, Ishvara Puri, Madhava Puri; having described this, the author sang the glory of his guru Thakura Narahari and of his nephew Raghunandana and concluded the Sutra-khanda.[^02-7]

*Raga varadi; disha: "O, o! — Murchha: — I would die taking on myself every ill that threatens Gora,[^02-8] taking on myself the ills that threaten His beauty and His virtues! In rapture He gave away prema, filling the whole world"*

**1.** Glory, glory to Sri Krishna Chaitanya and Nityananda,
glory, glory to Advaita Acharya, full of bliss!

**2.** Glory, glory to Narahari, [dear as] life to Gadadhara,[^02-9]
glory, glory to Srinivasa, worthy of bhakti!

**3.** All the beloved devotees of Chaitanya Gosani —
the feet of them all, [holding them] in my heart, I praise.

**4.** I shall tell of Chaitanya — listen attentively:
Damodara Pandita asked Gupta:[^02-10]

**5.** "Tell me, and I shall listen: for what reason did Gauranga appear?
To hear of it is a joy to my heart.

**6.** Why did He leave His dark colour and become Gaura?
Why in kirtana does He roll on the ground and cover His body with dust?

**7.** Why did He exchange the dress of a *nagara*[^02-11] for sannyasa?
Why does He wander from land to land as though in despair?

**8.** Why does He weep, calling out, 'Radha! Radha! Govinda!'?
Why does He go from house to house offering prema?

**9.** Tell me all this — the deepest secret,
hearing which even the most foolish of fools are saved."

**10.** Having heard, Murari says: "Listen, Pandita;
all this truth I will reveal to you.

**11.** In the Satya-yuga dharma has four quarters — so say the shastras;
in the Treta-yuga, I tell you, dharma has three quarters;

**12.** in the Dvapara-yuga, I tell you, dharma is half;
in the Kali-yuga, by the reckoning of dharma, one quarter.

**13.** Adharma grew, dharma dwindled;
[people] abandoned their own duty; there are neither varnas nor ashramas.

**14.** Kali became a sinful, impenetrable darkness;
all people sank, crippled by adharma.

**15.** Seeing that there was no dharma, the great sage Narada
was himself moved to compassion, [wishing] to save [the people of] Kali.

**16.** He thought: 'The serpent Kali has swallowed everyone,'
and he wished to establish dharma:

**17.** 'Without Krishna no one can establish dharma;
I shall surely bring Krishna into Kali, and quickly.

**18.** The devotee's wish is ever Govinda's wish:
such is the conclusion of the Vedas, agamas and shastras.

**19.** If I am in every way a servant of Krishna,
I shall bring into Kali the Lord, the king of the Yadus.

**20.** Let me first see what the Kali-yuga does,
and then I shall bring Krishna, the all-embracing dharma.

**21.** I shall bring all the gods with Him —
His weapons, His associates, all His intimates and retinue.'

**22.** Brahma and the other gods, Narada and the other sages,
the goddess Katyayani took birth on earth;[^02-12]

**23.** all who were in Dvaraka, in the line of Yadu,
took birth on earth, each by his own portion.

**24.** I shall tell it all — listen attentively —
how the descent upon earth came about.

**25.** The descent of Gora is the essence of all descents:
such compassion never was before.

**26.** The great sage Narada, grieved by the sorrow of others,
sings day and night the rasa of the tales of Krishna.

**27.** Thirsting for tales of Krishna, he roams the whole world,
but, looking over the world, he did not hear the name of Krishna.

**28.** Choking with the rasa of Krishna, he babbles in half-words,
now weeping, now laughing aloud;

**29.** he sings [Krishna's] virtues to the vina, tears stream from his eyes:
within and without, the sage is absorbed in the rasa of Krishna.

**30.** In such play of prema, rolling on the ground,
he did not hear the name of Krishna, though he went round the world.

**31.** Pained at heart, the sage was amazed:
he sees no way to save the people.

**32.** The black serpent Kali has bitten all people:
they burn unceasingly, blinded by the pride of maya.

[^02-1]: The Adi-khanda tells of the goddess Ganga's love for the young Nimai (so in Bhaktisiddhanta's survey of the parts and in the 1983 edition).
[^02-2]: So in Bhaktisiddhanta's text (A: "prabhura prana"); the 1983 edition has "by the Lord's grace".
[^02-3]: In the Madhya-khanda — the curse a brahmana pronounced upon the Lord (see Bhaktisiddhanta's summary of that chapter).
[^02-4]: Advaita Acharya's, at Shantipur.
[^02-5]: Literally "The Madhya-khanda ends: in the book, the manifestation of prema." Contrary to expectation, nothing is said here of the contents of the Shesha-khanda: Lochana Dasa only promises to tell of it; the meeting with Sarvabhauma he still assigns to the Madhya-khanda.
[^02-6]: Mahesha is Shiva. According to the *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika* and the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 6), Advaita Acharya is Maha-Vishnu, non-different from Hari, and at the same time Sadashiva; Lochana Dasa names here only the second aspect.
[^02-7]: The summary covers the whole section "Grantharambha" to the end of the Sutra-khanda (portions 02–08 of our translation). A few words are corrupt in the OCR and have been restored from the same summary in the *Khanda-vivarana* at the beginning of the edition.
[^02-8]: *Nichhani* is the rite in which one waves something around a beloved person to "take off" all harm and take it upon oneself; *balai laya*, "I take your ill upon myself", is an expression of tender love.
[^02-9]: So in Bhaktisiddhanta's text (A: "gadadhara-prana"); the 1983 edition has "Glory to Gadadhara Pandita, glory to Narahari".
[^02-10]: The 1983 edition has two couplets here instead of this one (a different redaction): "I shall tell of Chaitanya — listen attentively — how He descended in the age of Kali. Murari Gupta, the vaidya, knows the truth about the Lord; Pandita Damodara asked him." A (and the 1903 edition) has a single couplet coinciding with the first and last lines of B; we follow A.
[^02-11]: *Nagara*, an elegant townsman; here the fine dress of a fashionable householder that the Lord wore in His youth, as opposed to the garb of a sannyasi.
[^02-12]: In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition (A) couplets 22–23 are already narrative ("took birth"); in the 1983 edition they continue Narada's thought ("will take birth on earth"), and instead of "Narada and the other sages" it has "Sanaka and the other sages".

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 3 (portion 03)

*Narada comes to Dvaraka; Rukmini washes Krishna's feet and weeps, glorifying the greatness of His feet and the love of Radha; Krishna resolves to taste prema and to appear in the Kali-yuga, and reveals to Narada His form as Gaura; at Naimisharanya Narada tells everything to Uddhava*

**33.** The world is full of those who live only for belly and lust;
all people lie senseless, having forgotten Krishna.

**34.** With greed, delusion, lust, anger, intoxication and pride
they ceaselessly sprinkle their hearts as though with nectar.[^03-1]

**35.** Saying "I" and "mine", they perish for nothing;
who they themselves are, what is their own — they know nothing.

**36.** Seeing such sorrow of the people, the great sage
grew anxious at heart and pondered within himself.

**37.** Seeing no deliverance for people in the terrible age of Kali,
wandering on, he came to the gates of Dvaraka.

**38.** The Thakura of Dvaraka, God of gods, the crest-jewel of all,[^03-2]
had happily passed the night in the apartments of Satyabhama;

**39.** rising in the morning, He did all that was fitting
and let it be known that He would go to Rukmini's apartments.

**40.** Realizing what good fortune had come to her, Rukmini-devi
cannot contain herself — her body trembles.

**41.** She cleans the house, adorns herself;
instruments of many kinds are playing — joy without end;

**42.** auspicious jars full of water, lamps of ghee burning —
and at that time the Lord graciously arrived.

**43.** Mitravinda, Nagnajiti, Sushila, Subala[^03-3]
in bliss wave [lamps] around the Lord.[^03-4]

**44.** Bringing fragrant water to the Lord,
Sri Rukmini washes His feet.

**45.** Holding the feet, her own treasure, to her breast,
she gazes [at them] with love, now presses them to her face.

**46.** Pressing the holy feet to her heart, Rukmini weeps;
amazed, the Holder of the Discus asks:

**47.** "I cannot understand why you weep;
what are you weeping for, goddess? Tell Me what has happened.

**48.** You are dearer to Me than life — the whole world knows it;
who is dearer to Me than you? — tell Me yourself.

**49.** Has someone, out of neglect, failed to carry out your order?
Tell Me plainly, goddess, who has done wrong?

**50.** Only once before did I tease you[^03-5] —
is that grievance still in your heart today?

**51.** How much I pleaded with you then, humbly and piteously —
and still your hard heart did not soften."

**52.** Hearing such pitiless words from the Lord's mouth,
Rukmini answers tenderly, with a gentle reproach:

**53.** "My heart is hard; I cannot understand [Your words];
I have but one great good fortune — You are my life.

**54.** Your lotus feet are greater than You Yourself:
even now Shiva dances, drunk on their honey.

**55.** All that is in the world is known to You;
only the secret answer of love for [Your] feet You do not know.

**56.** If You place in Your heart the bhava of Radha,
then You will know what love for You is."[^03-6]

**57.** Hearing these words, the Lord was amazed at heart:
"What did you say, what did you say, goddess? Say it again.

**58.** I did not quite hear what you said;
can there be something that I do not know?

**59.** Hearing these wondrous words, I feel in My heart
a longing and some amazement.

**60.** Is there in the three worlds anything so hard to reach
that people marvel at seeing it and hearing of it?

**61.** From your lips I hear that there is something beyond My reach —
for joy My heart does not know what to do.

**62.** Tell Me, tell Me, tell Me, goddess — so I may be sure"; —
the greatness of the feet is sung by Lochana Dasa.

*Raga dhanashi; dirgha metre*

**63.** The goddess Rukmini says: "Listen, Lord, jewel of virtues,
think nothing else in Your heart:
what I weep for — that You do not know,
though all else You know.

**64.** What lies hidden in Your lotus feet, how great their power —
this You do not know well;
these feet will leave my house and go to another —
that is why my heart weeps.

**65.** Wherever, to the ends of the earth, the fragrance of these lotus feet is carried,
old age and death leave those regions;
and for those who live drinking the honey of these feet,
what are day and night, what are the seasons?

**66.** He who lovingly holds the lovely pollen of the lotus feet —
his feet I might touch only by great good fortune;
weeping I speak of all the pain in my heart —
I lay it all before You.
