**67.** You are the master of all; is there in the whole world
anyone who is master over You?
In love for Your feet this rasa is tasted —
this, Lord, is what I am telling You.

**68.** Radha alone knows this, having found that rasa of love —
all its joy, all its tenderness;
the devotees marvel at it, [speaking of it] day and night:
what rasa, what prema, what anuraga!

**69.** Brahma and the other gods and goddesses serve the feet of Lakshmi,
and she herself, out of her own love —
Kamala, a lotus in her lotus hand, beside herself with longing —
begs for service to these lotus feet.

**70.** Though Rama dwells on Your breast, not lying [apart] upon a bed,
face to face with You,
yet, longing for the sweetness of these feet, she does not value even that:[^03-7]
who then can tell the greatness of the feet?

**71.** Lakshmi, for all her own happiness, looks [at them] in supplication:
such is the grace of love for the feet;
Radha alone knows this — She who tasted [it] in Vrindavana:
there is no limit to Her good fortune.

**72.** And here is a riddle for the world: You are bound by Her virtues,
and even now Your heart repeats [Her name];
when You utter the name of Radha, Your eyes, I see, brim with tears:
such is the might of love for the feet.

**73.** These feet are in my house — and my heart rejoices,
yet I weep again for fear of separation;
Your holy lotus feet are stronger than You Yourself —
feel it and judge.

**74.** You are the object of his meditation, You are the knowledge of his samadhi,
You alone are his support in all things;
and such a servant of Yours sets his hope on Your feet[^03-8] —
this is what amazes me.

**75.** The feet whose maidservant Lakshmi is — why does she long for them?
Such is Your lordship:
though You are her master, You do not think of her good;
without a thought You pierce her with a spear.

**76.** Whoever with longing tastes the rasa of the honey of these feet
has a treasury inexhaustible and imperishable;
what then of Vani and Lakshmi — they think themselves blessed
by a mere touch of it.

**77.** The four liberations, beginning with salokya, follow at his heels,
yet he will not glance at them even from the corner of his eye;
he who has sunk into the rasa of prema — what else is dear to him?
He counts even Vaikuntha as nothing.

**78.** With joined palms I pray, Lord: of those lotus feet
make me a bee — grant me this boon;
from fear of separation from those feet this sinful life is ebbing away:
never leave my house."

**79.** Listen: Rukmini told the glories of the lotus feet —
it is love itself made manifest;
and so, by the Lord's mercy, with a trembling heart,
Lochana Dasa sings [His] glories.

*Raga dhanashi; madhya metre; disha: "Ah, ah, oh! — Murchha: — Here is a wondrous tale, its very name a blessing to the ear; hear further the tale of Gora's virtues"*

**80.** Hearing Rukmini's words, rejoicing at heart,
[the Lord] — His eyes, red lotuses, swimming in tears of compassion —

**81.** drew her to Him and speaks very softly,
sitting on the throne with Rukmini on His lap.

**82.** Taking her by the chin with His right hand, He looks into her face —
and the ocean of prema heaves in waves of nectar.

**83.** "Never have I heard anything so wondrous!
I Myself shall taste the happiness of prema," He said.[^03-9]

**84.** At that time Narada suddenly arrived;
the sage's face was joyless, his heart troubled.

**85.** Rising hastily, the goddess offered him water for his feet and arghya,
seated him on a splendid seat and asked after his welfare.

**86.** The Thakura, rising, embraced him tightly
and spoke to Narada with tender words.

**87.** For love both his eyes brim with tears;
the sage babbles, choking, he sways.

**88.** As he gazes [at the Lord], his eyes swim in tears of prema;
he wants to say something and cannot.

**89.** The Lord asked: "Sage, tell Me truly:
why are you so weak, why so troubled at heart?

**90.** You are dearer to Me than life, and I am your life;
seeing you in sorrow, I lose My senses."

**91.** Narada says: "Lord, what shall I say?
You are the Lord of lords, You are the inner witness of all.

**92.** To sing Your virtues is my nectar food;
thirsting for Your virtues, I go round the whole world.

**93.** Going round the world, I did not hear the name of Krishna:
people are drunk with their own intoxication, having forgotten You.

**94.** All people lie senseless, blinded by pride;
seeing people without Krishna — that is my grief.

**95.** I see no way to save the people;
my mind thinks only of this, unceasingly.

**96.** I have laid before You all the grief that was in my heart;
by the grace of Your feet all else is happiness."

**97.** Smiling, the Lord says: "Listen, great sage:
you have forgotten all that happened before —

**98.** how Katyayani made a vow
in her talk with Mahesha for the sake of mahaprasada.[^03-10]

**99.** And Rukmini has said something unprecedented;
hearing it, I was moved and made a vow:

**100.** I shall taste the happiness of prema and give it to the people to taste;
I shall show a humble mood in the Kali-yuga;

**101.** together with the devotees, practising bhakti,
I, though I am Ishvara, shall distribute My prema;

**102.** I shall reveal the sankirtana of My virtues and names,
I shall be born in the house of Shachi in Navadvipa;

**103.** My body will be golden and tall, My arms reaching the knees,
beautiful as Sumeru, beyond compare."

**104.** As He said this, the Lord became golden;
seeing it, Narada was filled with great longing.

**105.** A body beautiful as Sumeru, in the rapture of prema —
so Lochana tells of Gora's first manifestation.

*Shri-raga; disha: "Ah, Gauranga, glory, glory! — Murchha: — Ah, my prema for Gauranga — nectar! Ah, my [treasure], glory, glory!"*

**106.** Gazing [at Him] with a jubilant heart, the sage Narada
sheds tears in a thousand streams.

**107.** A radiance surpassing millions of moons, a brilliance of millions of suns,
a beauty surpassing millions of Kamadevas: so shines the peerless Gaura.

**108.** The dazzling radiance of the body — one cannot look at it;
the sage sits with eyes shut, trembling all over.

**109.** Restraining His radiance, the Lord looks at Narada
and, seeing him powerless, calls him in a loud voice.

**110.** Coming to himself, the sage [is lost] in meditation on that form,
and his eyes thirst to see [it] again.

**111.** The Thakura says: "Sage, listen, greatly fortunate one:
your path is barred by nothing, everywhere you are honoured.

**112.** Proclaim in the worlds of Shiva, Brahma and the others:
I shall appear in the Kali-yuga as Gaura.

**113.** I shall reveal the sankirtana of virtues and name,
I shall spread the happiness of the rasa of bhakti and prema for Me.

**114.** Hundreds upon hundreds of branches — the paths of bhakti are without limit;
turning people towards one, I shall spread prema.[^03-11]

**115.** Together with My own devotees and associates,
born on earth, [I shall fulfil] the longing for prema-bhakti."

**116.** Hearing such words from the holy mouth, Narada,
by the grace of [the Lord's] feet, was freed from all sorrow.

**117.** The sage Narada went on, playing his vina,
absorbed in these thoughts:

**118.** "What a wondrous form of Gora I have seen!
What red eyes full of compassion I have seen!

**119.** What a radiance I have seen — sweeter than nectar!
What a sweet smile on the holy lips I have seen!

**120.** Of all the descents there have been, this is the essence;
never have I seen such a treasure-house of prema.

**121.** Blessed is my birth, blessed are my eyes:
I have seen the form of Gora, His serene face.

**122.** Never have I seen such an ocean of mercy:
the heart cannot forget, my eyes are satisfied."

**123.** Pondering thus, the sage goes on his way,
and in the forest of Naimisha he meets Uddhava.

**124.** Uddhava, rising respectfully, offered water for the feet and arghya
and lay prostrate on the ground, falling at his feet.

**125.** He thinks the day auspicious and himself fortunate:
"At an auspicious hour the sage has come to the forest of Naimisha."

**126.** Narada lifted him up, embraced him tightly,
kissed him and smelled the crown of his head.

**127.** Uddhava brought him a seat,
[Narada sat,] and, smiling, [Uddhava] tells what is in his heart:

**128.** "Blessed is my birth, special is this day;
let me lay before you one long-standing pain of my heart.

**129.** Long ago Vyasa, here in the forest of Naimisha,
having studied the Vedas, was not freed from the heaviness of his mind;

**130.** by your grace he heard the hidden truth
and for the deliverance of people composed the *Bhagavata*.[^03-12]

**131.** You alone know the truth, you know the truth about the Lord;
understanding the Lord's purpose, reveal the future:

**132.** how will people be saved in the Kali-yuga —
wrapped in sin, the eyes of their hearts blind?

**133.** I know what the dharma of people is in the Satya, Treta and Dvapara yugas;
but in the terrible Kali-yuga there is nothing but sin.

**134.** Be merciful, tell me, dispel my doubt:
who is more merciful than you?"

**135.** Rejoicing at heart, the sage says, smiling:
"Well have you asked, Uddhava, servant of Hari!

**136.** I will tell you the deepest secret:
the very same grief was in my heart too.

**137.** Now I have understood: the Kali-yuga is blessed;
apart from the people of Kali, there is no one else so blessed.

**138.** The dharma of the Satya and the other yugas is a severe discipline;
the dharma of the Kali-yuga is the excellent Hari-nama.[^03-13]

**139.** By the sankirtana of the name and virtues they are freed from bondage;
they go about dancing and singing, free from the fear of Yama.

**140.** And here is another wonder — listen attentively —
which I saw with my own eyes in Dvaraka.

**141.** Speaking of this with Rukmini, the Lord
resolved in His heart: 'I will unfold My prema.'

**142.** He was sitting on the throne with Rukmini on His lap —
at that time I arrived, troubled at heart.

**143.** Seeing me in sorrow, the Lord asked me:
'Why do I see you like this?'

**144.** Falling at His feet, I told Him what was in my heart;
the Lord, His face serene, said smiling:

**145.** 'Rukmini has told the greatness of love for [My] feet;
hearing it, I am overwhelmed — so great is [this] longing.

**146.** I shall taste the happiness of prema and give it to the people to taste;
I shall show a humble mood in the Kali-yuga.

**147.** The terrible Kali-yuga is sinful, without dharma;
to bring people to understanding, I shall become very humble.'

**148.** A body full of prema, golden, tall, of lovely hue,
a broad chest, arms reaching the knees —

**149.** as He said this, the Lord became golden
and vowed to give away His prema.

**150.** What I saw, what I heard, I have told you;
I shall go and proclaim it throughout the world.

**151.** Taking birth on earth out of longing for prema-bhakti,
so wondrous [the Lord] will appear in the Kali-yuga."

**152.** Hearing Narada's words, Uddhava, beside himself,
weeps, fallen at his feet, overwhelmed with joy:

**153.** "You have told me such a wondrous thing —
as though life were breathed into a lifeless body.

[^03-1]: So in Bhaktisiddhanta's text (A: "amiya"): people take these passions for nectar. The 1983 edition has "sprinkle their hearts with poison".
[^03-2]: Literally "the Thakura of Dvaraka, crest-jewel of the God of gods".
[^03-3]: Mitravinda and Nagnajiti are among Krishna's principal queens (*Bhag.* 10.58). Sushila and Subala may also be names of queens, or epithets ("good-natured, youthful"); we take them as names.
[^03-4]: *Nirmanchhana*: the rite of waving a lamp or other object around a beloved person to ward off all harm.
[^03-5]: Cf. *Bhag.* 10.60: Krishna in jest told Rukmini that she should have chosen a king equal to her as husband, and she fainted from grief.
[^03-6]: Literally "then You will know the marks of Your own love", i.e. the love directed to You. This directly echoes how Gaudiya Vaishnavas understand the reason for Sri Chaitanya's appearance: Krishna wishes to know the greatness of Radha's love, the sweetness of Himself that She tastes, and Her bliss, and therefore comes, accepting Her bhava and lustre (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 1.6 and ch. 4). Bhaktisiddhanta says the same in his summary of this section: Krishna descends as Gaura "accepting the bhava of Radha".
[^03-7]: Rama is Lakshmi. Cf. *Bhag.* 10.16.36: Lakshmi performed austerities desiring to touch the dust of Krishna's feet.
[^03-8]: Probably Shiva is meant (cf. v. 54): he contemplates the Lord in samadhi and himself longs for His feet.
[^03-9]: The 1983 edition likewise: "'I shall taste the happiness of prema,' He Himself said." This is the first statement of the vow spoken in full in v. 100.
[^03-10]: The story of the mahaprasada, Shiva and Katyayani (Parvati) is told by Lochana Dasa further on, in the account of Narada's visit to Kailasa.
[^03-11]: Literally "let people be turned in one direction" — towards the one prema-bhakti.
[^03-12]: In the *Bhagavata-purana* (1.4–1.7) Narada instructs the dejected Vyasa at his ashrama on the bank of the Sarasvati (Shamyaprasa), while it is Suta who recites the *Bhagavata* to the sages at Naimisharanya; Lochana Dasa, following his source, joins these accounts.
[^03-13]: Cf. *Bhag.* 12.3.52: what was attained in the Satya-yuga by meditation, in the Treta by sacrifice and in the Dvapara by worship is attained in the Kali-yuga by the chanting of Hari's name.

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 4 (portion 04)

*Narada parts from Uddhava and comes to Kailasa; Narada's account of the mahaprasada: Uddhava's words on the remnants of the Lord's meal, Narada's service to Lakshmi, the dance of Shiva after tasting mahaprasada, the Earth's complaint; Parvati's vow to give mahaprasada to all living beings; the Lord of Vaikuntha approves her vow and promises to appear in the Kali-yuga as Gaura*

**154.** You have refreshed my very body with your words"; —
and Narada went on, rejoicing, playing his vina.

**155.** The dialogue of Narada and Uddhava from the *Jaimini-bharata* —
hearing it, Lochana Dasa is mad with joy.

**156.** Whoever does not believe my words,
let him examine the book — the thirty-second chapter.[^04-1]

*Raga bhatiyari; disha: "Ah, my life — Gorachand!"*

**157.** The sage Narada goes on, and his vina sings [His] virtues;
hearing it, his heart overwhelmed, he falls again and again.

**158.** Now he weeps, now laughs aloud,
now trembles, now stammers in half-words;

**159.** now he roars and slaps his arms like a wrestler;[^04-2]
crying "Gora, Gora!" he weeps, his heart restless.

**160.** He cannot forget Gora's sweetest prema;
his body blazes with radiance like the sun.

**161.** For prema he cannot walk, his heart exults —
and in the twinkling of an eye he was at Shiva's Kailasa.

**162.** "I shall see Mahesha!" — and his joy grew:
"I shall tell him of Krishna, all in due order.

**163.** There is no such joyful news in the three worlds:
the treasure of Vrindavana [the Lord] will reveal in the Kali-yuga;

**164.** the prema for which Shiva, Brahma and Ananta pray
the fallen and unruly [people] of Kali will enjoy.

**165.** I shall tell Mahesha this wondrous news —
hearing it, the lord will be greatly pleased.

**166.** By Katyayani's grace I shall take the dust of her feet —
by the grace of [those] feet I utter Hari's name."[^04-3]

**167.** Musing thus, he came to Mahesha's gate;
seeing him, Nandi and Mahakala rose respectfully.[^04-4]

**168.** Bowing, Nandi went inside,
where Parvati and Mahesha were in their inner apartments,

**169.** and announced that Narada was at the gate;
both came out at once with joyful hearts.

**170.** Seeing Narada, the lord greets him with a smile;
the sage, a wise devotee, fell at his feet.

**171.** Mahesha, like no other, knows the greatness of Vaishnavas:
showing his love, he honours Narada.

**172.** Embracing him tightly, he seated him beside himself;
the sage, falling at the goddess's feet, greets her.

**172+1.** Narada, rich in austerity, was taken by the hand and led,
and with honour seated on a seat.[^04-5]

**173.** With a mother's tenderness Katyayani asks Narada:
"Tell me of your welfare, dear great sage.

**174.** You know the truth of the fourteen worlds;
from where have you come today — to our good fortune?"

**175.** Narada says: "Hear wondrous news;
you are mother and father, [come] for the deliverance of the world.

**176.** You have forgotten a former secret;
clasping your feet, I shall now remind you of it.

**177.** I shall tell you everything from beginning to end;
having heard it, you yourself will show me grace.

**178.** Once Uddhava asked the Lord:
'When You withdraw from sight, will the earth remain?

**179.** Will devotees remain on this earth?'
Hearing this, the Thakura explained to him the yoga of what He Himself is:[^04-6]

**180.** 'I am water, I am land, I am earth and trees,
I am gods and gandharvas, I am yakshas and rakshasas;

**181.** I am origin and dissolution, the life of all beings;
I pervade all — how can I vanish?'[^04-7]

**182.** Hearing such words of the Thakura, Uddhava,
striking his breast with his hand, speaks his own realization:

**183.** 'You are the all-pervading Lord, this I know;
but greater than You [Yourself] are Your two feet.

**184.** Whoever is caught in the net of the moonlight of Your toenails —
what more can I say? — his glory cannot be put into words.'

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(11.6.46):*

**185.** "Adorned with the garlands, scents, garments and ornaments that You have enjoyed,
we, Your servants who eat Your remnants, shall surely conquer Your maya."[^04-8]

**186.** 'My strength is this: I, Hari's servant, eat the remnants;
I conquer Your maya — all my hope is in Your remnants.'

**187.** Hearing such a conversation of the Thakura and Uddhava,
I felt pain in my heart:

**188.** for so long I have known the path [of bhakti],
yet until today I did not know for certain [the power of] such remnants.

**189.** By the strength of the remnants Hari's servant gains strength —
that is why in the Lord's presence he praised them so.[^04-9]

**190.** Such mahaprasada I have never tasted —
I realized in my heart that the Lord had deprived me.

**191.** How might I taste this mahaprasada?
By what means could I win fate's favour?

**192.** Absorbed in this thought, I went to Vaikuntha
and served Lakshmi-devi in every way.

**193.** The goddess, pleased, said graciously:
'Ask a boon — I shall give it,' and she made a vow.

**194.** Hearing the vow, my heart took hope,
and I had her confirm those kind words once more.

**195.** With a pleading voice and folded palms I said:
'For long there has been a great pain in my heart.

**196.** Everyone knows that Narada is your servant,
yet he has not tasted the remnants of the Great Lord's meal.[^04-10]

**197.** Give me a handful of the Lord's prasada —
this is the boon I ask; look on me with kindness.'

**198.** Hearing this, Lakshmi-devi, astonished,
began to speak humbly:

**199.** 'There is no order of the Lord to give His remnants to anyone;
how can I, O sage, transgress His order and give you the remnants?

**200.** Wait a little for my sake:
in time I may be able to give, having saved [some].'

**201.** So sweetly the queen spoke;
I answered, 'Good, good,' understanding the matter myself.

**202.** Some days passed, and one day the Lord, in a tender mood,
took the goddess by the hand and seated her beside Him.

**203.** Smiling, He talks lovingly with her;
the goddess, asking leave to speak, with fear at heart,

**204.** bowed and said: 'I have a petition;
my heart is afraid — I am in distress and abashed.

**205.** Remove my trouble, Lord, protect Your maidservant;
clasping Your feet I beg: hear me, treasury of virtues.'

**206.** Lakshmi speaks piteously to the Lord, in fear;
He looks at Sudarshana with a surprised smile.[^04-11]

**207.** The disc Sudarshana trembles and says pleadingly:
'Lord, I know nothing of Lakshmi's trouble.'

**208.** Lakshmi says: 'Sudarshana is not to blame:
because of Narada's words my heart has withered.

**209.** For twelve years he served me without revealing [his aim];
pleased, I made a vow —

**210.** "Ask a boon, I shall give it," I said: "Truly, truly";
and the sage made that word fast for ever.

**211.** The boon he asked is Your remnants;
but what power have I to transgress Your order?

**212.** So I have told You — calamity is upon me;
protect Your maidservant, Lord, remove my trouble.'

**213.** Understanding all, He said: 'Listen, Lakshmi:
a very grave matter is what you have told.

**214.** Give it to him in secret — so that I do not know.'
Hearing the Lord's words of leave, she was glad.

**215.** After some days that Mother of the world
called me and gave me mahaprasada.

**216.** By Lakshmi's grace I obtained mahaprasada
and, my desire fulfilled, I ate the mahaprasada.

**217.** A light surpassing millions of moons, the beauty of millions of Kamadevas,
the splendour of millions of suns — so wondrous did I become.

**218.** At the touch of mahaprasada my splendour grew a hundredfold;
playing my vina, happy, I came to Kailasa.

**219.** Seeing me, lord Mahesha
said with a smile: 'What a wondrous look you have today!

**220.** Your splendour is so wondrous that it amazes the eye;
why do you have such a form today? Tell me truly.'

**221.** I told him everything from first to last;
having heard it, Mahesha reproached me:

**222.** 'Having got such rare mahaprasada,
you, O sage, ate it alone without giving me any!

**223.** You have come again with love to see me —
why did you not bring so rare a treasure?'

**224.** Hearing Mahesha's words, ashamed,
[I] look down with lowered eyes, rubbing nail against nail.

**225.** 'There is a particle of mahaprasada,' I said, and gladly gave it;
without a second thought the lord put it into his mouth.

**226.** In bliss the great lord Mahesha dances —
beneath his feet the earth shudders.

**227.** Under the weight of prema Mount Sumeru sways,
the earth trembles, alarm is everywhere.

**228.** The lord of yoga trembles in prema, forgetting himself;
under Mahesha's weight the earth sinks to Rasatala.

**229.** Ananta's hoods press against the Tortoise's back;
bending his neck, Kurma stares fixedly [upward].[^04-12]

**230.** Under the weight all the bearers of the quarters bend their necks;[^04-13]
at [Shiva's] roar the shell of the universe cracks.

**231.** Unable to bear Mahesha's weight, the goddess [Earth]
hurried to Mahesha's abode.

**232.** With joined palms the Earth says to Katyayani:
'Under the weight of Mahesha's dance I am giving up my life.

**233.** Help me, if creation is to be saved:
I see calamity has fallen upon the whole world.'

**234.** Hearing the Earth's piteous words, Parvati
went quickly to where Pashupati was.

**235.** The king of gods dances fully absorbed in rasa;
with harsh words she breaks Mahesha's rapture.

**236.** The lord came to his senses, grieved,
and, seeing Parvati, speaks to her harshly:

**237.** 'What have you done, what have you done, goddess — what an outrage!
To break my rapture is the same as killing me.

**238.** I have no enemy in the three worlds but you:
why did you end such bliss of mine?'

**239.** Hearing this, the goddess says piteously again:
'Look, lord, at the Earth before you.

**240.** Under the stamping of your feet she is sinking to Rasatala,
creation is perishing — that is why I spoke harshly.

**241.** I have offended — forgive my fault, great one.'
Smiling, Mahesha dismissed the Earth.

**242.** And again the goddess asks entreatingly:
'Lord, I ask one thing — a doubt troubles me:

**243.** every day you dance absorbed in the rasa of Krishna —
why then does the earth sink to Rasatala today?

**244.** The splendour of millions of suns, fierce rays —
the universe cannot hold so wondrous a radiance.

**245.** Why this wondrous, endless bliss today?
Tell me in detail, lord, rich in virtues.'

**246.** Mahesha says: 'Hear a joyful tale:
the great sage gave me the Lord's prasada.

**247.** Rare in the three worlds is [a taste of] what is offered to Vishnu,
still more the nectar of His lips, unknown to the Vedas.[^04-14]

**248.** Such mahaprasada I have eaten —
my birth is fruitful, this is an auspicious hour.'

**249.** By Narada's grace — the touch of mahaprasada:
he told her this auspicious story, a delightful treasure.

**250.** Hearing the lord's words, Mahamaya says:[^04-15]
'Now at last I know how great your kindness is!

**251.** You hold me as half your body — it is all deceit;
now your false love is exposed!

**252.** Having got such rare mahaprasada,
you, god, ate it alone without giving me any!'

**253.** Helpless with shame, the Trident-bearer says:
'This treasure is not for you, Bhavani.'

**254.** Hearing this, angered, the primal Shakti says:
'My name is Vaishnavi; I serve Vishnu.

**255.** I make a vow before all —
and you will know whether there is kindness for me in the Lord's heart:

**256.** this mahaprasada I shall give to the whole world —
by my vow even jackals and dogs will receive it.'[^04-16]

**257.** When Katyayani made such a vow,
the Lord of Vaikuntha, hearing it, came quickly.

**258.** Rising respectfully, the goddess bowed
and with tearful eyes made her plea to Him;

**259.** with an aching heart, sighing, she speaks —
and with a joyful heart this Lochana Dasa tells it.

*Raga vibhasha*

**260.** Softly the Lord says: 'Do not be agitated, goddess;
what is this conduct of yours?
The whole world is blind in the bonds of your maya —
that is why My creation goes on.

**261.** You are My primal shakti, you know bhakti,
you are by nature My prakriti;
I am not apart from you, nor, I say, are you apart from Me;[^04-17]
whatever you do is your grace.

**262.** By worshipping Hara and Gauri all people come to know Me:
Hara and Gauri are My own body;
when your heart is pleased, all maya vanishes,
the division into 'mine' and 'another's' vanishes.[^04-18]

**263.** Such is your vow — these remnants of Mine
you will give to all without hindrance;
by the mere fragrance of mahaprasada all will be freed from bonds;
it will do away with restrictive rules.

**264.** Hearing the Lord's words, Katyayani says again:
'If there is kindness for me in Your heart,
surely give [Your] remnants: let all beings eat them,
let the three worlds receive them without hindrance.'

**265.** Again the Jewel of virtues says: 'Hear, goddess Katyayani:
I shall keep your vow — there is a story about it;
it is an ancient secret; I tell it to you privately —
and the anxiety of the fever of worldly life will go.

**266.** No one knows the truth of this ancient secret:
when the gods churned the ocean —
Mandara the churning rod, the serpent Ananta the rope —
from the friction a shoot arose.[^04-19]

**267.** That is My wish-fulfilling tree: let suppliants ask
whatever each holds in mind;
whoever wants whatever treasure receives that treasure —
it disappoints no one in his hope.

**268.** There, in a divine radiance, within that beautiful tree,
Sri Chaitanya is present in bodily form;[^04-20]
that is My natural form, a king of pure compassion —
all others are not equal to Him.

**269.** All the avataras — He is their abode and shelter,[^04-21]
[from Him they come] for the sake of pastimes, arts and delights.
I shall remain on earth, Lord and master of the three worlds,
and shall spread mercy.

**270.** Especially in the Kali-yuga, revealing sankirtana,
I shall take a human form;
My body will be golden; I shall keep your vow
and spread the highest love.

**271.** This secret of My heart I have told you —
keep it guarded in your mind:
the essence of all avataras, Gora, the avatara of Kali —
by My virtues I shall deliver the people.'"

**272.** The dialogue of Vishnu with Katyayani is in the *Brahma-purana*,
set forth in the *Utkala-khanda*;
King Prataparudra, an ocean of all virtues,
made it widely known.[^04-22]

**273.** "Why do you not remember this?" —
laughing, says the king of sages. —
"The Lord ordered me to proclaim
the work of the avatara of the Kali-yuga:

**274.** when the Kali-yuga comes, all of you will be born on earth
as your own portions, under other names;[^04-23]
all those lords of the worlds, with all the associates,
will be born in brahmana families."

**275.** Hearing Narada's words, the Trident-bearer rejoiced,
the goddess Katyayani rejoiced;
the abode filled with joy, all say "Hari, Hari!" —
a joyful clamour arose.

[^04-1]: A reference to the source of the dialogue, the *Jaimini-bharata* (v. 155). Which text by this name Lochana Dasa has in mind is unclear.
[^04-2]: *Malasat*: slapping the shoulders and thighs with the palms, as wrestlers do to challenge an opponent; in the Gaudiya biographies one of the signs of ecstatic rapture.
[^04-3]: Narada humbly names the grace of Katyayani (Parvati) and Shiva as the source of his devotion. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Shiva is the best of Vaishnavas (*Bhag.* 12.13.16); so Bhaktisiddhanta also calls him in his summary of this section.
[^04-4]: Nandi and Mahakala are Shiva's servants and doorkeepers.
[^04-5]: V. 172+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition it is in a footnote, as an additional reading of one printed book. In the 1983 edition the first line of v. 172 also differs: "He embraces [him] tightly with joy in his heart" — without the words "seated him beside himself". The text does not say who leads Narada — Shiva or the goddess.
[^04-6]: The conversation of Uddhava with the Lord before His departure is in the *Bhagavata*, canto 11 (11.6.43–50 and the following *Uddhava-gita*, where the Lord explains yoga). Literally "explained yoga concerning His own doings".
[^04-7]: Cf. the *Bhagavad-gita*, chs. 7 and 10. The Lord pervades everything by His energies and dwells in all as the Paramatma, while remaining a person distinct from creation and inconceivably one with it (*achintya-bhedabheda*); there is no identification of God with the world here.
[^04-8]: In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition every quoted shloka is followed by his prose order of the words (*anvaya*) and a Bengali translation; on this page they are almost illegible. "Your maya" is the external energy of the Lord, which holds living beings captive; the Lord Himself is not subject to it.
[^04-9]: "Hari's servant" (*hari-dasa*) here is Uddhava.
[^04-10]: "The Great Lord" (*mahaprabhu*) here is Narayana, the Lord of Vaikuntha, not Sri Chaitanya.
[^04-11]: Sudarshana is the Lord's disc, the guardian of His apartments; the Lord wonders whether he has offended Lakshmi.
[^04-12]: The earth is upheld beneath by the serpent Ananta (Shesha) on his hoods, and beneath him by the Tortoise (Kurma).
[^04-13]: "The bearers of the quarters" (*digvaha*): the *diggajas*, the elephants said to support the world in the eight directions.
[^04-14]: "The nectar of His lips" (*adharamrita*): the remnants of food touched by the Lord's lips; this is mahaprasada in the proper sense.
[^04-15]: Mahamaya is a name of Parvati (Durga). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Durga is the external energy of the Lord, carrying out His will as a shadow follows the movements of the body (*Brahma-samhita* 5.44); below the Lord Himself calls her His shakti (vv. 260–261).
[^04-16]: Cf. the tradition of Puri: everything offered to Jagannatha is then offered to Vimala-devi (Parvati) in Jagannatha's temple, and only then becomes the mahaprasada that is given to all without distinction.
[^04-17]: An energy is inseparable from its possessor (*shakti* and *shaktiman*). In the Gaudiya understanding Durga is the external energy of the Lord (*bahiranga-shakti*, maya), the shadow of His internal energy (*chit-shakti*) to which Radha and the eternal consorts belong; the Lord Himself is not subject to maya — she serves His will, keeping captive those who have turned from Him.
[^04-18]: So Lochana Dasa: "Hara and Gauri are My own body." According to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching Shiva is not different from the Lord in source, but, in contact with tamo-guna, becomes distinct, as milk turned into curd (*Brahma-samhita* 5.45); Durga is the Lord's external energy (5.44). "The division into mine and another's" is the false "I" and "mine" (cf. v. 35) created by maya; there is no question of the difference between the soul and God disappearing.
[^04-19]: Literally "a hair" (*loma*); from the following verses, the shoot from which the wish-fulfilling tree (*kalpa-taru*) grew (cf. the Parijata, which came out of the churned ocean).
[^04-20]: Lochana Dasa probably connects this tree with the Kalpa-vata, the sacred banyan by Jagannatha's temple in Puri, honoured as a wish-fulfilling tree; the whole dialogue (v. 272) comes from the traditions of Utkala (Orissa) and Purushottama-kshetra.
[^04-21]: Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 2: Krishna (and Gaura as Krishna) is the *avatari*, the source of all avataras.
[^04-22]: The *Utkala-khanda* is the account of Purushottama-kshetra (Puri) known as part of the *Skanda-purana*; a Purushottama account is also found in the *Brahma-purana*. Prataparudra was the king of Orissa (reigned c. 1497–1540), a devotee of Sri Chaitanya. What exactly Prataparudra did to spread this account is not clear from the text.
[^04-23]: Cf. Kavi Karnapura's *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*, which states which eternal associates and gods appeared as whom in Gaura-lila. The words "in brahmana families" are the author's generalization: not all the Lord's associates were brahmanas by birth (Murari Gupta, for example, was a vaidya, and Haridasa Thakura was born in a Muslim family).

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 5 (portion 05)

*Narada comes to Brahma and gives him the Lord's words about His appearance in the Kali-yuga; Brahma tells of the doubt of Sanaka and the other Kumaras, of the Lord's answer in four verses, and of how the* Bhagavata *was given through Narada; Brahma recalls Garga Muni's words about the colours of the avataras in the various yugas and, following the* Bhagavata, *describes the avataras and the worship of the Satya-, Treta- and Dvapara-yugas*

**276.** The sage Narada went on, his vina sounding —
its sound sprinkles sweet rasa;
as if a stream of sweet nectar filled the ears,
delighting the hearts of the people of the three worlds.

**277.** Going, he forgets himself, he cannot keep to the road,
his face reddened with love;
he feels no weariness of the way, drops of sweat on his brow —
and so he reached Brahma's abode.

**278.** Seeing him, Brahma hastily, with great joy at heart,
rose to greet the sage;
the sage bows, falling at his feet;
raising him, Brahma embraced him.

**279.** He asked of his welfare, deeming himself blessed by his coming,
with the love of long separation:
"It seems to me, looking at your bright face,
that you, fortunate one, will tell a secret.

**280.** Words from your mouth are nectar to the ear;
speak — I shall listen; let my heart be soothed:
what news of the worlds? Tell the song of the Lord's virtues —
what have you seen, what have you heard?"

**281.** In due order Narada tells his story, full of ardour:
his lips quiver, his body sways,
his eyes are full of tears, his lips are red —
as he begins to speak, his joy is doubled:

**282.** "Hear wondrous news. You are the maker of all creation;
I roam the universe that bears your name.[^05-1]
According to each yuga people practise the dharma of that yuga,
but in the Kali-yuga sin is rampant.

**283.** Seeing the people at the end of Dvapara, all in sorrowful grief,
I dreaded Kali;
with an anguished heart I went straight to the Lord
and asked with great boldness:

**284.** 'In the sinful age of Kali by what means
will You deliver the people? Tell me, Lord:
the brahmanas are without the Vedas, dharma has waned in all —
in my heart is a great doubt.'

**285.** Hearing the piteous words, the Lord, jewel of virtues, says:
'Put away the anxiety of your heart:
I shall deliver the people of Kali, I shall spread devotion to Me,
I shall descend there Myself.

**286.** Charity, vows, austerity, dharma and all other works —
all this I shall place in Hari's name;
see, Kali is full of faults, but note one great virtue:
release from bondage — by My sankirtana.[^05-2]

**287.** Proclaim: let Shiva, Brahma and the rest
all be born on earth when Kali comes;
I, the embodiment of compassion, shall be born on earth,
becoming golden according to the yuga.'"[^05-3]

*Shubhachhanda, raga pahida; disha: "Glory, glory to the moon Gauranga, risen in Nadia in the age of Kali! — Murchha: — Ah, hear the story of my Lord, whose virtues lit up these three worlds! Ah, hear the story of Gaurangachand! Ah, what is this, ah!"*

**288.** Hearing such words, lord Brahma
planted in his heart a sprout of the nectar of prema.

**289.** His cheeks thrill, tears stream from his eyes;
beside himself with joy, Brahma embraced the sage.

**290.** Brahma says: "Hear, best of great sages:
by your grace my heart is made clear today.

**291.** In the snare of worldly things all are blind in the bonds of maya;
by your grace they are freed from their bonds again.

**292.** You care only for the deliverance of people;
I shall tell you something of the past — about myself.

**293.** The sages Sanaka and the others, my sons,
opening their hearts, said to me:

**294.** 'You are the Lord's beloved son;
whatever we ask, explain to us its essence.

**295.** The Lord is inconceivable, imperishable, eternally blissful, He is Brahman,
subtle, lord of all lords, all-pervading, [the ground of] dharma;

**296.** infinite, beyond the gunas, spotless, formless,
without beginning, middle or end — so reason judges.[^05-4]

**297.** How then, being such a Lord, is He born on earth —
unborn, yet being born, does the deeds of ordinary men?

**298.** In Vrindavana He held the rasa with the cowherd wives,
like people given to the play of amorous pleasure.[^05-5]

**299.** Be they women or men, He is the Self in all:
why then such dalliance, as though He were not content?

**300.** Great is this doubt in my heart;[^05-6]
tell the truth, Four-faced one, clear away the confusion.'

**301.** When Sanaka and the others expressed such a doubt,
hearing it, I was amazed at heart.

**302.** My face darkened with anxiety:
this conduct of the Lord is beyond my grasp.

**303.** This is beyond Vedanta; who knows the truth?
Brahmas like me there are hundreds upon hundreds.

**304.** Just as I was about to voice this thought,
at that very moment the Lord came in the form of Hamsa and spoke:

**305.** in four verses He gave me the answer,
and that answer I passed on to them all.[^05-7]

**306.** All those great souls were pleased
and, satisfied, went wherever each wished.

**307.** Those four verses are to me the vessel of all rasa;
there is none in the universe who knows their truth.

**308.** Some time later Vyasa, in the Naimisha forest,
set forth all that is in the *Bharata* and the Puranas;

**309.** nothing was left to say, yet the heaviness of heart did not lift,
and he fell into distress.

**310.** Vyasadeva swooned in the forest;
knowing this, the Lord was filled with compassion.

**311.** Calling me, He gave these four verses:
"Take this supreme treasure and go to Vyasa.

**312.** Vyasa does not know the truth of My deeds;
let him compose the *Bhagavata* following these verses.

**313.** This *Bhagavata* you shall tell to Narada:
Sarasvati, Sharada, will speak with his tongue."[^05-8]

**314.** Therefore I say: hear, best of sages —
age after age you alone show mercy to living beings.

**315.** For the deliverance of living beings you are the great soul;
the *Bhagavata* is the divine scripture — there is no other treasure.

**316.** The *Bhagavata* is untouched by worldliness — it is an independent Person;[^05-9]
not understanding this, the fool takes it for mere book-learning.

**317.** Such a statement of the *Bhagavata* about Krishna's descent
Garga Muni made at the name-giving ceremony;[^05-10]

**318.** now I have remembered Garga Muni's words,
the account of [the Lord's] colours according to the four yugas.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(10.8.13):*

**319.** "Taking bodies yuga after yuga, He had three colours —
white, red and likewise yellow; now He has become dark (Krishna)."[^05-11]

**320.** In the Satya-yuga people knew Him as white,
in the Treta as of rosy lustre, and His name was Yajna;

**321.** now this son of Nanda is dark,
and at the last there will be an avatara of yellow colour.[^05-12]

**322.** Whoever doubts the verse, saying that its order is broken,
understands it thus: three colours for four yugas.

**323.** White, red, yellow, dark — these are four colours,
and the yugas are four, there are no others.

**324.** Otherwise consider: in which yuga is [the Lord] golden?
Speaking hastily, one will not dispel the doubt.

**325.** I shall say a little about this — listen:
I shall now explain it to the devotees.

**326.** The *Bhagavata* speaks of this in the eleventh canto:
the king asked the sage Karabhajana.[^05-13]

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(11.5.19); the king said:*

**327.** "At what time, of what colour and form is that Bhagavan,
by what name and by what rite is He worshipped by men? Tell us this."

**328.** At what time does Bhagavan take what colour,
what is His name, at what time [did He appear];

**329.** at what time what dharma, what kind of people,
by what rite do they worship, what pleases [Him]?

*Sri Karabhajana said (11.5.20–22):*

**330.** "In the Krita, Treta, Dvapara and Kali yugas Keshava
is of various colours, names and forms, and is worshipped by various rites.

**331.** In the Krita He is white, four-armed, with matted locks, clad in bark,
bearing a black deerskin, sacred thread, rosary, staff and waterpot.

**332.** People then are peaceful, without enmity, friendly, even-minded;
they worship God with austerity and control of mind and senses."

**333.** The sage says to the king: "Listen attentively
to how people worship in the Satya and the other yugas.

**334.** In the Satya-yuga He is white and bears the name Hamsa;
four-armed, His dharma is austerity, He wears matted locks and bark,

**335.** staff, waterpot, antelope skin and sacred thread;
and people are by nature peaceful, without enmity, even-minded."

*So (11.5.24–25):*

**336.** "In the Treta-yuga He is red, four-armed, with a triple girdle,
golden-haired, the soul of the three Vedas, marked by sacrificial ladles and the like.

**337.** Then people, righteous and learned in the Veda, worship Him —
God Hari, who comprises all the gods — by the knowledge of the three Vedas."

**338.** That Lord in the Treta-yuga takes a red colour:
four arms, a triple girdle, sacrificial ladles in His hands,

**339.** on His head hair like molten gold;
the Lord who comprises all the gods Himself performs sacrifice.

**340.** The three Vedas are His soul; He bears the name Yajna;
those versed in dharma worship Him by the rules of the Vedas.

*So of the Dvapara (11.5.27–28, 31):*

**341.** "In the Dvapara Bhagavan is dark, in yellow garments, with His own weapons,
distinguished by the Shrivatsa and other marks.

**342.** Then mortals seeking knowledge worship Him, the Supreme Person,
bearing the marks of a great king, by the Vedas and tantras, O king.

**343.** Thus in the Dvapara, O lord of the earth, they praise the Lord of the world
by the rules of various tantras; and how [they praise Him] in Kali — hear."[^05-14]

**344.** In the Dvapara Bhagavan takes a dark colour:
the Shrivatsa and Kaustubha on His body, yellow garments;

**345.** the marks of a great king, sovereign of kings, shine on Him;
fortunate people worship Him by the Vedas and tantras.

**346.** Thus in every yuga [comes] the yuga-avatara,
and people practise the dharma of their yuga.

**347.** Satya, Treta and Dvapara — three yugas have passed,
and the colours were white, red and dark.

[^05-1]: *Brahmanda*, literally "Brahma's egg", the universe.
[^05-2]: Cf. *Bhag.* 12.3.51: "O king, Kali is an ocean of faults, but it has one great virtue: by chanting of Krishna one is freed from bondage and attains the Supreme."
[^05-3]: Cf. below (vv. 319–321 and 349): according to the *Bhagavata*, the Lord in each yuga takes a form of its own colour; in Kali, golden.
[^05-4]: This is the reasoning of the Kumaras, who formerly adhered to the impersonal Brahman (cf. *Bhag.* 3.15.43: the fragrance of the tulasi at the Lord's feet turned them to bhakti). In the Vaishnava understanding "beyond the gunas" and "formless" mean "free of material qualities and material form": the Lord has an eternal spiritual form.
[^05-5]: This is the doubt of the Kumaras, not the author's assertion. Parikshit voices the same doubt (*Bhag.* 10.33.27–28), and Shukadeva answers it (10.33.29–37): the rasa is the purest prema, not mundane passion; the Lord is self-satisfied and dwells as the Witness in the hearts of the gopis and their husbands. Bhaktisiddhanta stresses that the love of the gopis is the highest form of prema, free from all lust.
[^05-6]: A has "my heart", although many Kumaras are asking; so in both editions.
[^05-7]: In the *Bhagavata* the Lord in the form of Hamsa answers the Kumaras' question about detaching the mind from sense objects (11.13), while the four verses that are the essence of the *Bhagavata* (2.9.32–35) the Lord reveals to Brahma before creation. Lochana Dasa joins these accounts.
[^05-8]: In the *Bhagavata* (2.9; 1.5–1.7) Brahma passes on what he heard to Narada, and Narada instructs the dejected Vyasa.
[^05-9]: Cf. *Bhag.* 1.3.43: when Krishna departed to His abode, the *Bhagavata* rose like the sun for the people of Kali who have lost their sight.
[^05-10]: *Bhag.* 10.8: Garga Muni, the priest of the Yadus, gives names to Krishna and Balarama in Nanda's house.
[^05-11]: Garga Muni's words at Krishna's name-giving. The Gaudiya acharyas (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 3.36–40) take "yellow" (*pita*) as the golden form in which Krishna appears in the Kali-yuga as Sri Chaitanya, and "now He has become Krishna" as the appearance of Krishna Himself, the original Lord. Further on (portion 06) Lochana Dasa discusses this verse at length.
[^05-12]: "At the last" (*parisheshe*), i.e. in the Kali-yuga, the last of the four; so Lochana Dasa takes the "yellow" colour of the verse.
[^05-13]: In the *Bhagavata* (11.2–11.5) King Nimi (Videha) questions the nine sage-yogendras; Karabhajana answers about the yuga-avataras.
[^05-14]: Verses 11.5.29–30 (the hymn of the Dvapara) the author does not cite. The verse on Kali (11.5.32) is quoted at the beginning of portion 06.

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 6 (portion 06)

*Brahma continues: the Lord in the Kali-yuga is the golden Gaura of whom the* Bhagavata *speaks (11.5.32); explanation of the words "krishna-varna" and "sangopanga", of Garga Muni's verse on the four colours and of the word "tatha"; Krishna is not a yuga-avatara but Bhagavan Himself, and in the Dvapara and the Kali it is He Himself who descends; testimonies of the* Gita, *the "Thousand Names" and the* Bhavishya-purana; *the greatness of the Kali-yuga and of sankirtana*

**348.** Of the three colours in three yugas the sage has told;
now hear attentively the account of the Kali-yuga.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(11.5.32):*

**349.** "Him who utters [the name] 'Krishna' but is not dark in lustre,[^06-1] with His limbs, sub-limbs, weapons and associates,
the intelligent worship with sacrifices consisting chiefly of sankirtana."

**350.** He in whom there are these two syllables, "Krish-na",
the *Bhagavata* calls "krishna-varna".[^06-2]

**351.** And in lustre He is "not dark" — hear, everyone:
this is why we sing, "Gora, Gora!"

**352.** With His limbs, sub-limbs, weapons and all His associates —
with all of them the Lord descended.

**353.** Balarama is called the limb — so I say "with limbs";
the lesser limbs are ornaments — so "with sub-limbs".[^06-3]

**354.** The weapons — Sudarshana and the rest — and all the associates,
Prahlada, Narada — all came together [with Him].

**355.** The servants and maidservants of former descents —
He descended with all His own: how many shall I name?

**356.** Of all this the Vaishnavas speak from their realization:
what name each had there and what name now.[^06-4]

**357.** How can an ordinary man know this?
The mind of the fallen cannot believe it.

**358.** For this reason the sage said:
only he who is intelligent will know it.

**359.** The sacrifice consisting of sankirtana is revealed as dharma —
he who is intelligent finds supreme joy in it.

**360.** Therefore I say: whoever does not accept this
has the explanation "three colours for four yugas".

**361.** [For him] "dark in lustre" and "dark in colour" merge into one,
while the colours of the other two yugas are not so:

**362.** in the Kali and in the Dvapara — in two yugas — one colour;
in two yugas the colour is one — that is his meaning;

**363.** in Satya and Treta there are two colours, white and red,
and then, in Kali and Dvapara, one colour.[^06-5]

**364.** Why do you say that the order is broken in Garga Muni's words?
The order is not broken — listen, there is great charm in it.

**365.** To express past, future and present,
he speaks of three times within the four yugas:

**366.** Satya and Treta are past, Dvapara is present;
in the Dvapara is the descent of Krishna, and His name is Krishna.

**367.** That is why Garga Muni says "now",
and within the past tense he means the future.

**368.** Knowing what is certain to be,
one speaks of its future in the past tense:

**369.** the learned accept the past tense in the sense of the future —
it points to certainty; that is the hint.[^06-6]

**370.** And yet the sage put the word "tatha" ("likewise") there:
after saying "white, red", what use is "tatha" in the account?

**371.** By the word "tatha" it is said: just as the white and red named before,
so in the Kali-yuga Hari will be yellow.

**372.** Now, in the Dvapara, this one has become Krishna;
so Garga Muni expressed three times within the four yugas.

**373.** Whoever contemptuously rejects my words,
let him say why the *Bhagavata* uses the word "tatha".

**374.** This is what I say — hear my words;
says Lochana: do not push my words aside.

**375.** Hear yet another wondrous explanation of the verse —
it alone is right, it is the highest proof.

**376.** To this explanation there is a remarkable objection:
Krishna a yuga-avatara? That is quite impossible.

**377.** The other yuga-avataras are described as portions and parts,
[but Krishna] is Bhagavan Himself; the *Bhagavata* is witness.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(1.3.28):*

**378.** "All these [avataras] are portions and parts of the Purusha, but Krishna is Bhagavan Himself;
age after age they gladden the world troubled by the enemies of Indra."

**379.** How then can one call Krishna a yuga-avatara?
Why then does the *Bhagavata* say these words?

**380.** The moon of Vrindavana is not a yuga-avatara:
Krishna is the most complete Brahman, says the *Bhagavata*.[^06-7]

**381.** For this reason I shall say something — listen;
let no one disregard it; pay attention.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(10.8.13):*

**382.** "Taking bodies yuga after yuga, He had three colours —
white, red and likewise yellow; now He has become Krishna."

**383.** Garga Muni spoke this with the deepest understanding —
how can we who lack understanding grasp it?

**384.** He who is intelligent learns [its meaning] from devotees;
intelligent people accept it as proof.

**385.** The sage named four colours in four yugas —
an account of the three times: past, future and present.

**386.** He wishes to express three times within four yugas —
all this Vyasa says in one verse.

**387.** Satya, Treta, Dvapara and the Kali yuga;
white, red, yellow and dark — are said of the four yugas.

**388.** If the four yugas had been [named as] four times,
then the three [other] avataras too would have come in order,

**389.** and the statement would be sequential:
as the avataras [come], so the words would follow.

**390.** That is why people see a break of order in such a verse;
[but] by the word "tatha" Garga Muni indicates the future time.[^06-8]

**391.** Who is the avatara, and whose are the four colours?
Who is the source of avataras — how is this to be judged?

**392.** Bhagavan Himself is born in the Yadu dynasty,
[while at other times] He descends to earth by His portions.

**393.** Why do you explain [the verse] making one word the qualified and the other the qualifier?
This is the doubt here, and hence the ambiguity.[^06-9]

**394.** In all four yugas [come] partial avataras,
and all of them have a colour according to the yuga.

**395.** To establish dharma and destroy irreligion
there is a partial avatara in every yuga.

**396.** But in the Dvapara [comes] Bhagavan Hari Himself,
the crest-jewel of avataras, above all.

**397.** "Now He has become Krishna," says Garga Muni:
[this is] Shyamasundara Krishna, and "krishna" here is not a colour.

**398.** In every Dvapara [comes] a portion with the name and colour "krishna";
[this time] the Lord Himself assumed that form — hear, this is the meaning.[^06-10]

**399.** As Krishna in the Dvapara, so [in Kali] Gaurachandra:
in Kali and Dvapara these two are independent.

**400.** In these two yugas [comes] one full avatara;
Vyasa gave an example of this.

*So in the* Brihat-sahasranama-stotra:

**401.** "Worshipping you, O Shambhu, I always accept this boon:
in the Dvapara and the following yugas, appearing as a portion among men and others,
you, by your own invented scriptures, turn people away from Me
and conceal Me, so that this creation may increase ever more."[^06-11]

**402.** I shall say more — hear the *Bhagavad-gita*,
the Lord's own words from His holy mouth.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavad-gita *(4.8):*

**403.** "To protect the righteous, to destroy the wicked
and to establish dharma, I appear age after age."[^06-12]

**404.** Protection of the righteous, establishment of dharma,
destruction of irreligion — this is the meaning of what was said.

**405.** "Yuge yuge" — I Myself take birth;
in these two yugas I am born Myself.

**406.** With one word "yuga" I mean [the age], and the other "yuga" means [a pair]:
people explain [them] as qualifier and qualified.

**407.** "Yuga" qualifies "yuga" — therefore I say "pair":
one is the Dvapara yuga, the other the Kali yuga.[^06-13]

**408.** Why do you take "yuge yuge" as the four yugas?
Krishna's descent is full; why do you call it partial?

**409.** Of those four yugas it is spoken elsewhere;
that too I shall tell — attend to it.

*So in the same (4.7):*

**410.** "Whenever dharma declines, O Bharata,
and irreligion rises, then I manifest Myself."

**411.** At whatever time, in whatever yuga, dharma declines
and irreligion rises — know that at that very time

**412.** I manifest Myself; [this] descent in every yuga
[takes place] by means of a portion.[^06-14]

**413.** This is what I say — hear my words;
says Lochana: do not push my words aside.

**414.** "That in the Kali-yuga Gora is Krishna I have come to know;
you have dispelled my great doubt.

**415.** Hear another wonder — the essence of the Kali-yuga:
in any ashrama the dharma of sankirtana delivers people.[^06-15]

**416.** Charity, vows, austerity, fire offerings, Vedic study, self-restraint —
these rules and observances [serve only] desires and worldly aims;

**417.** hearing the shruti's promises of fruits, all fall into the bonds of maya;
the glory of [the Lord's] name and virtues the wretched blind do not know.

**418.** The soul bound by the thread of karma wanders on,
and there is no end [for it]: karma cannot be restrained.

**419.** [Only] at the dissolution of the world are all the bonds of karma loosed —
but these bonds are loosed when one asks about Krishna.[^06-16]

**420.** Such sankirtana of [the Lord's] virtues is the dharma of the Kali-yuga;
not knowing its essence, people call [Kali] terrible and sinful.

**421.** How could [anyone] abolish the dharma of the yuga, sankirtana?
Who but the Lord establishes dharma?

**422.** [Such is] the Lord's former vow in the *Gita*, in His words:
for what reasons the Lord descends.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavad-gita *(4.8):*

**423.** "To protect the righteous, to destroy the wicked
and to establish dharma, I appear age after age."

**424.** Protection of the righteous, destruction of irreligion,
establishment of dharma are manifested in every yuga.

**425.** Accept this, then: in the Kali-yuga the dharma is sankirtana,
and the avatara of Kali is Gora; it is never otherwise."

**426.** Saying this, [Brahma] embraces the sage;
beside himself with joy, Brahma forgets himself.

**427.** He says one thing — and another rises [in his heart] by the splendour of Gora's virtues:
he longs to delight all his senses [with them].

**428.** "Hear more: in the Thousand Names of the Lord
names from two [periods] of one [appearance] stand in one place.[^06-17]

*So in the* Mahabharata, *Shanti-parva:*

**429.** 'Golden in colour, with limbs like molten gold, of beautiful form, adorned with sandal;
taking sannyasa, equipoised, peaceful, devoted to steadfastness and peace.'

**430.** His body is molten gold, His lustre golden;
having taken sannyasa, He is the supremely wise.

**431.** Hear Krishna's vow in the *Bhavishya-purana*:
'In Kali I shall be born' — three times this word is said.

*So in the* Bhavishya-purana:

**432.** 'I shall be born, I shall be born, I shall be born — there is no doubt:
in Kali, at the beginning of sankirtana, I shall be the son of Shachi.'[^06-18]

**433.** Hear attentively yet another wonder
and consider in your mind the essence of the dharma of the Kali-yuga.

**434.** Everyone says the Kali-yuga is sinful:
irreligion is manifest, dharma is weak in conduct.

**435.** [But] the sankirtana of Hari's name is its dharma,
and this name of Hari is the essence of all dharmas.

**436.** The fruits of charity, vows, austerity, fire offerings, knowledge and japa,
and liberation without effort, the power of the name alone bestows.

**437.** Even a worldly man who, amid his pleasures, thinks of the name —
[the name] first gives him enjoyment and then bestows devotion to Hari.[^06-19]

**438.** If a person of faith sings Hari's virtues,
the Lord, leaving all pleasures, runs after him.

**439.** Such sankirtana of Krishna's name and virtues
He made the dharma for the sinful Kali-yuga.

**440.** The nature of the yuga is one thing, and the dharma of the yuga that I name is another:
in the sinful Kali-yuga this is the highest dharma.

**441.** If you ask why — because sin is hard to cut:
[for this] He revealed the great sword, the sankirtana of the name.

**442.** Why do the people of Satya and the other yugas ask to be born in Kali?
[Because] in the Kali-yuga they will become devoted to Hari's name.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(11.5.38):*

**443.** 'The people of Krita and the other yugas, O king, desire to be born in Kali,
for in Kali there will be [people] wholly devoted to Narayana.'

**444.** Why, in the descent of Krishna, appearing with all His potencies,
did He not give prema-bhakti to people of sinful hearts?[^06-20]

**445.** Tell me: in what other yuga was there such mercy?
Which avatara gives prema to those who do not worship?

**446.** For destroying sin there are dharma, rites, holy places —
but do you know whether the righteous obtain such a treasure?

**447.** Know, then: Kali is the essence of all yugas,
and there is no dharma other than the dharma of sankirtana."

**448.** Such a discourse Brahma set forth;
having heard it, Narada skilfully plays his vina.

**449.** Hearing such a wondrous conversation of Brahma and Narada,
the heart of this Lochana Dasa rejoices.

[^06-1]: *Krishna-varna*: literally "dark in colour" or "whose utterance (*varna*) is 'Krishna'"; *tvisha akrishna*, "not dark in lustre". In the edition the verse is followed by Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya and translation (OCR damaged): *krishna-varna* — He who describes Krishna; "not dark in lustre" — golden; the "limbs" (*anga*) — Nityananda and Advaita; the "sub-limbs" (*upanga*) — Srivasa and others; the "weapons" (*astra*) — Hari-nama and the like; the "associates" (*parshada*) — Gadadhara and others. In translation: "The intelligent worship with the sacrifice of sankirtana the Mahapurusha who always reveals the virtues of Krishna and is golden in lustre, together with His limbs, sub-limbs, weapons and associates."
[^06-2]: A play on words: *varna* means both "colour" and "letter, sound". Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 3.53.
[^06-3]: Lochana Dasa's interpretation: the "limb" (*anga*) is Balarama, i.e. Nityananda; the "sub-limbs" (*upanga*) are ornaments. Krishnadasa Kaviraja (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 3.66–76) and Bhaktisiddhanta in his anvaya take the "limbs and sub-limbs" as Nityananda, Advaita, Srivasa and the other associates, and the "weapon" as the holy name.
[^06-4]: Cf. Kavi Karnapura's *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*, which states which associates of Krishna appeared as whom in Gaura-lila.
[^06-5]: Vv. 360–363 set out the opponent's view: for him verse 11.5.32 speaks of a dark avatara of Kali, so that Kali and Dvapara have one colour, and the four yugas only three colours.
[^06-6]: Lochana Dasa has in mind the form *asan* ("were") in Garga's verse: the "yellow" one, not yet appeared, is spoken of in the past tense because His appearance is certain.
[^06-7]: Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 2 and 3: Krishna is the original Lord (*svayam bhagavan*), the source of all avataras; when He descends Himself, the yuga-avatara too is present in Him.
[^06-8]: The reasoning of vv. 385–390 is condensed: had the verse listed four colours one after another as four past yugas, the order would be strict; since Garga lists white, red and yellow in the past tense and says "now" of the dark one, the order seems broken; but "tatha" shows that yellow is the colour of the future, of the Kali-yuga.
[^06-9]: The sense of the verse is unclear. Perhaps the words "but Krishna is Bhagavan Himself" (*Bhag.* 1.3.28) are meant: which of them is the qualified and which the qualifier (cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 2, where it is discussed which word in this verse is subject and which predicate).
[^06-10]: Lochana Dasa takes *krishnatam gatah* not as "has become dark" but as "has become Krishna": the yuga-avatara of every Dvapara is dark, but in this Dvapara Krishna Himself took his place. Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 3.5–10: Krishna appears once in a day of Brahma, at the end of the Dvapara (of the twenty-eighth *chatur-yuga*), and in the Kali that follows appears as Sri Chaitanya.
[^06-11]: This verse (from the *Padma-purana*) is also quoted in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 6.182), as the Lord's order to Shiva to spread Mayavada scriptures in Kali. In Bhaktisiddhanta's translation in this edition: "I, ever worshipping Shambhu, shall accept this boon: you, appearing as a portion among men in the Dvapara and the following yugas, turn people away from Me by invented scriptures and conceal Me, so that creation may increase." Lochana Dasa cites the verse for the words "in the Dvapara and the following yugas" as an example of the Lord Himself descending in these yugas; this is his own interpretation.
[^06-12]: Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya: "the righteous" are those devoted to the Lord's service; "the wicked" are those hostile to devotees; "to establish dharma" is Himself to practise the dharma of each yuga and teach it to living beings.
[^06-13]: A play on words: *yuga* means both "age" and "pair". According to Lochana Dasa, *yuge yuge* in *Gita* 4.8 means "in the pair of yugas" — Dvapara and Kali, when the Lord Himself descends — while the partial avataras of every yuga are the subject of verse 4.7 (vv. 409–412).
[^06-14]: So Lochana Dasa. The Gaudiya commentators usually refer both 4.7 and 4.8 to the Lord's descents in general; here the author distinguishes: in every yuga a partial avatara, in Dvapara and Kali the Lord Himself.
[^06-15]: A has "in an ashrama" (any of the ashramas); the 1983 edition, "under the supreme shelter".
[^06-16]: Line 1 is obscure; probably "the bonds of karma fall away only at the dissolution of the world [, and even then not for ever]".
[^06-17]: That is, one verse of the *Vishnu-sahasranama* gives names from two periods of Gaura's life. In Bhaktisiddhanta's translation (the text is partly damaged): the first four names ("golden", "with limbs like gold", "of beautiful form", "adorned with sandal") are marks of the pastimes of household life; the rest, of the pastimes in sannyasa: "taking sannyasa", "equipoised" (absorbed in contemplating Hari's mysteries), "peaceful" (firm in the great sacrifice of Hari-kirtana), "devoted to steadfastness and peace" (to the mahabhava that restrains the non-devotees).
[^06-18]: So Bhaktisiddhanta understands this verse: "In Kali, at the beginning of sankirtana, I shall be born as the son of Shachi — I shall be born, I shall be born, I shall be born, there is no doubt." In the 1983 edition the first line differs: "Be born on earth, be born, be born in the form of devotees" — the Lord's command to the gods.
[^06-19]: Cf. *Bhag.* 2.3.10: even one who has all desires, or desires liberation, should worship the Supreme Lord with intense devotion.
[^06-20]: Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 3.14–20 and ch. 4: Krishna reflects that for long He has not given prema-bhakti to the world, and appears as Gaura, giving prema to all without discrimination; cf. Rupa Gosvami's verse *anarpita-charim chirat…*

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 7 (portion 07)

*Narada answers Brahma: on service to the Vaishnavas, on the hidden prema of the gopis and how the Lord will spread it in Kali; the rejoicing of the three worlds; Narada sees the coming of Kali, hears the voice of Jagannatha and comes to Nilachala; Jagannatha describes Goloka and sends him to Gaura; Narada with the Lord of Vaikuntha, who calls Himself a portion of Gaura; in Goloka Narada beholds the bathing of Gaurasundara*

*Raga sindhura*

**450.** Narada says: "Brahma, what more shall I say?
All that you have said is my very heart.

**451.** Wandering in the bonds of karma, [a soul] roams for so many kalpas!
If by fortune a little service to the Vaishnavas falls to its lot,

**452.** and it hears from them the exalted secret word,
then it keeps it with the utmost care, attentively.

**453.** Then, freed from bondage, it becomes devoted to Krishna
and will not touch with a finger the four liberations, salokya and the rest.

**454.** And beyond that is prema-bhakti, the bhava of the gopis:
who is qualified even to speak of all that?

**455.** By their rasa the Lord, the Master of the three worlds, is conquered —
yet to worldly people [their love] seems like consorting with an unchaste woman.[^07-1]

**456.** Who can tell of their prema-bhakti?
For the sake of their virtues Uddhava begs to be born a bush or a creeper.

*So in the* Srimad-Bhagavata *(10.47.61):*

**457.** 'Oh, that I might be anything at all — a bush, a creeper or a herb — in Vrindavana,
[to serve] the dust of the feet of those who, abandoning kinsmen hard to abandon and the path of the noble,
attained the way of Mukunda, which the shrutis seek!'[^07-2]

**458.** That Lord on whose feet Brahma and Mahesha meditate,
whom the best of yogis and sages seek and find not even a trace of;

**459.** whose feet are served by countless Lakshmis,
the honeyed radiance of whose feet is beyond the reach of words;

**460.** whose greatness the four Vedas ever sing,
finding no limit to His endless glory and virtues;

**461.** whose bed is the great Shesha —
such a Lord served the gopis.

**462.** There are hundreds of other devotees too,
but only the gopis so conquered Him that He became obedient to them.

**463.** Where is Krishna, the Supreme Self — and so hidden a prema —
and where the gopis, forest-dwellers, 'unchaste', 'lustful'?[^07-3]

**464.** Such is the truth of bhakti I long to understand:
there is no bhakti more hidden than this.

**465.** This bhakti the Lord will spread in the Kali-yuga —
that which even Lakshmi and Ananta have never heard of.

**466.** Tell everyone, Brahma, throughout Brahmaloka:
'Take birth by your portions in the Kali-yuga!'"

**467.** Having said this, with jubilation in his heart,
the great sage Narada went on his way, says this Lochana Dasa.

*Raga mallara, tripadi metre; disha: "O my life, Gorachand!"*[^07-4]

**468.** Narada Muni set out; the roar of his vina is heard,
softly flows a song that delights the ear;
as if it sprinkled nectar on the minds of the world's people —
the worlds thrilled with joy.

**469.** "Jaya, jaya! Haribol!" — all were immersed in bliss;
a proclamation went through the three worlds:
"With [His] weapons and associates, joyfully He will take birth —
Gora, the avatara for the Kali-yuga!

**470.** Such an ocean of mercy my eyes will see;
He will sprinkle nectar on my body.
Jaya, jaya, Jagannatha! Together with the devotees
He will spread devotion to Himself.

**471.** Blessed, blessed is the Kali-yuga, blessed are all its people,
[blessed] the earth, and Nadia upon it;
blessed is Mishra Purandara, in whose house
Gora-raja has taken birth.[^07-5]

**472.** With these devotees, joyfully singing the virtues of Hari —
conches, mridangas and kartals sounding —
through the fourteen worlds, raining down the rasa of prema,
He will spread the kirtana of [His] virtues.

**473.** The rasa of the virtues of Vrindavana — love, which is all in all —
He will taste Himself and give to all;
to gods, nagas and men, to everyone down to the chandala,
He will give to drink that for which He awakens their thirst."

**474.** Bliss upon bliss are [His] virtues; hear: good upon good —
the treasure of Vrindavana is revealed;
the Lord of all the worlds has taken birth on earth —
in bliss this Lochana Dasa forgot himself.

*Raga varadi; disha: "My Lord, O my life, oh, Gorachand!"*

**475.** Yogis, great sages, Indra, Chandra and the rest in their worlds,
hearing [the news], filled with bliss, dance in delight.

**476.** Narada, full of bliss, roams in delight —
it seems to people as if a dead tree had put forth shoots.

**477.** Roaming thus, he suddenly
sees that dharma has been overturned in people's conduct:

**478.** abandoning charity, vows and austerity, all people
with body, speech and mind honour [only] their wives.[^07-6]

**479.** Reflecting on this, the sage knew for certain:
this is the Kali-yuga, there is no doubt of it.

**480.** "That for which a proclamation went through the three worlds —
to whom shall I tell it? — here it is, the Kali-yuga has come!"

**481.** Troubled, the sage sat down in meditation,
and suddenly an auspicious voice arose in the sky:

**482.** "I am Jagannatha, the Daru-brahman,[^07-7] in Nilachala,
on the shore of the ocean, for the deliverance of people.

**483.** Do you not remember the former account?
Through Katyayani's vow you received My command.[^07-8]

**484.** Go, go, king of sages, to the city of Nilachala
and act according to the command of Jagannatha."

**485.** Narada Muni set out with joy in his heart;
the sound of his vina arose, filling the world.

**486.** Crying "Ha, ha, Jagannatha!" he runs in love
and beheld the moon-like face of the King of the three worlds —

**487.** the abode and shelter of all avataras,
a gracious face full of the rasa of all arts.

**488.** Falling at His feet, the sage says with joined palms:
"Be merciful, Jagannatha — the Kali-yuga has come!

**489.** All people have fallen into the most terrible sin;
devoted to lust and belly, they have gone astray, in great sorrow."

**490.** Hearing this, the Lord, smiling a little, spoke;
touching him with His hand, He told him in private:

**491.** "I tell you this supreme secret:
at My word go to Goloka.

*Raga pahida, tripadi metre*

**492.** Above Vaikuntha there is an abode whose name is Goloka;
Sri Gaurasundara is king there;[^07-9]
the women are Lakshmi and the others, the one Purusha is Hari,
and all the subjects are full of happiness.

**493.** Radha and Rukmini — these two queens;
all the nagaris are their portions;
hundreds upon hundreds of branches of bhakti, holding the power of these two,
serve [Him] as their attendants.[^07-10]

**494.** And the goddess Satyabhama, incomparable in beauty and virtue,
the limit of the rasa of all clever wit;
blessed with lila, play and loveliness, with the rasa of all arts —
the supreme woman of the three worlds.

**495.** She who is called Music — with rhythm and the movement of tones —
the world praises as Shabda-brahman;
she is called the fifth Veda — [for one] who understands the distinctions of notes —
she is understanding itself, present equally in all.[^07-11]

**496.** The men are portions of the Lord, all of Vaishnava lineage;
the city, full of rasa, is named Ranga.[^07-12]
Such is the greatness of that [abode] — who has the power to tell it?
I cannot tell it with one mouth.

**497.** All the gopis who danced the rasa in Vrindavana
serve [Him] with Radha at their head;
all who were in Dvaraka follow Rukmini —
and all the others who taste rasa.

**498.** There is nothing there but bhakti; unceasingly they sing [His] glory;
though independent, they are dependent [on Him];
all are liberated, and yet, like ordinary people,
they practise bhakti as if humble.

**499.** The four liberations, salokya and the rest, are the power of the Lord of Vaikuntha;
devoid of bhakti, they are independent in themselves;
full of the riches of Lakshmi, they keep no humility —
bhakti alone depends on Another.

**500.** Sugar itself does not know its own sweetness —
another enjoys it;
such is the station of liberation: it bars the path of bhakti —
above all is the yoga of prema-bhakti.

**501.** That abode, beyond the reach of the Creator, is My home;
out of mercy I have come here.
Behold Sri Chaitanya, the Lord of all, golden and tall of stature,
and the pain of your heart will go.

**502.** In whatever form you see Me there, in that form I shall come here;
I shall spread the kirtana of [My] virtues,
remove all suffering, spread the joy of prema
and deliver the people of Kali."

**503.** Narada Muni set out, having heard these wondrous words:
"This speech is beyond the reach of the Vedas:
above Vaikuntha, further still, I shall see Goloka,
whose glory is sung in all the worlds.

**504.** Of liberation and supreme liberation, of the reasoning of the *Bhagavata*
I have heard all the secret words;
what is unknown to the world and the Vedas, unknown, unmanifest —
today I shall see it manifest there."

**505.** In love the sage runs; the sound of his vina is heard —
the people of Vaikuntha rejoiced;
reaching the gate of Vaikuntha, overwhelmed with bliss,
he sings an auspicious song of [His] virtues.

**506.** He saw the Lord of Vaikuntha with all His associates,
seated on a jewelled throne;
falling at His feet, the sage bows down,
and the Lord, raising him, embraced him.

**507.** Smiling, the Lord says: "What is in your heart?
Tell Me quickly, sage, what is in your mind.
My heart is eager: I shall fulfil your word,
I shall make the unseen seen."

**508.** With joined palms the sage says: "You are the Indweller of all,
what more shall I tell You?
That form of Yours of which You told me within, in the form of the Daru-brahman —
that is what I [wish] to see."

**509.** Again the Jewel of virtues says: "I tell you in private:
that form is [My] original, innate form;
His maya,[^07-13] His reflections — hundreds of avataras —
worship Him with the greatest zeal.

**510.** I am His bodily expansion, pervading all lands,
the all-pervading Vishnu, all in all;
Lakshmi is My attendant, and the four liberations too —
this connection also I tell you.

**511.** I, Vishnu, am His portion; Lakshmi is [His] wealth;
this Vaikuntha is a portion of [His] Vaikuntha;
the four liberations are a shadow of [true] freedom; all, clothed in bhakti,
serve that Master — that Lord of Vaikuntha.[^07-14]

**512.** Radha alone is the prakriti, Her form is prema itself;
to Her the supreme Purusha is subject;
in Vaikuntha there is one abode named Maha-Vaikuntha —
there is the union of the three qualities of shakti.[^07-15]

**513.** Firm is My word, without guile: Gaurahari
is the manifest wish-fulfilling tree of mercy.
Go, sage, go to that Mahaprabhu,
the teacher of all the worlds."

**514.** The king of sages set out, his vina singing Hari's virtues,
his body, helpless with bliss, trembling;
his whole frame thrilled, from foot to head,
tears of prema flooding both his eyes.

**515.** Drunk with the wine of prema, now he is amazed,
now he calls out "Gauranga!",
now he goes half a step, now looks back again and again,
now he weeps, now he runs on.

**516.** Suddenly a breeze blew, cooling the whole body;
a radiance as if surpassing millions of moons;
with the fragrance of the lotus feet the body's bonds grow loose;
whatever one sees there is the glory of Kama.[^07-16]

**517.** Many Madanas run obediently to serve;
one sees no one there without prema;
there is no knowing of day or night, no seeing of "mine" and "another's";
all are joyful, without sorrow.

**518.** Walking is the play of dance, speech the art of music,
a glance of the eyes is attraction;
there is no movement without play, no companionship without bhava;
the bodies there are formed of rasa.

**519.** Bodies are of consciousness and bliss, the ground is chintamani,
all the trees there are wish-fulfilling trees,
all the cows are like new kamadhenus,
the bushes and creepers are what Uddhava and others long for.

**520.** All the trees are wish-fulfilling trees; among them is one beyond compare,
with jewelled altars on both sides;
on it a golden throne, where Lord Gauranga sits
and sweetly, tenderly, softly smiles.

**521.** An auspicious pot with [mango] twigs right by the throne
touches the great toe of His left foot;
jewelled lamps burn, as if with the rays of the sun,
illuminating and filling the world.

**522.** Radhika on the right, keeping Her maidservants near,
a jewelled pitcher in Her hand;
on the left Rukmini, keeping her companions near,
fills a golden pot with water bright as jewels.[^07-17]

**523.** Nagnajiti draws water and gives it into Mitravinda's hands,
Mitravinda into Sulakshana's hands,
she gives it into Rukmini's hands, and the goddess pours it on the Lord's head —
a bathing with the water of the heavenly river.

**524.** Tilottama draws water and gives it into Madhupriya's hands,
Madhupriya into Chandramukhi's hands,
she gives it into Radhika's hands, and Rai pours it on the Lord's head —
a bathing with the water of the Ganga.

**525.** Satyabhama is near, holding divine scents in her hands,
divine garlands, garments and ornaments;
Lakshmana, Subhadra and Bhadra, obedient to Satyabhama,
in turn hand [everything] to her.

**526.** And all the other divine women, by hundreds on every side,
with divine ornaments and divine gifts,
holding jewelled bouquets, stand before the Lord,
crying the auspicious "Jaya, jaya!"

**527.** Such is the bathing of the Lord of Goloka — there is no other;
the Agamas have told the great meditation [on Him]:
a body golden-fair, a mantra of four syllables;[^07-18]
the Lord of Vaikuntha is by nature dark.

**528.** A dark body with four arms has the Lord of Vaikuntha,
in His four hands four weapons;
but the Lord radiant with gold, golden-limbed, softly smiles —
hear the essence: His body is two-armed.

**529.** At that moment the sage, seeing Gora, the Jewel of virtues,
fell enraptured at His feet;
he cannot open his eyes — and again he longs to look;
he bathed [Him] with the tears of his eyes.

**530.** Having finished His bath, the Lord, smiling, speaking very softly,
lifted Narada up and took him on His lap;
doubt and anxiety went, the pain of his heart was broken;
