the Lord speaks loving words very softly.

[^07-1]: That is, worldly people, judging by appearances, see in the gopis' love for Krishna only a breach of marital fidelity. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following the *Bhagavata* (10.33.26–39) and the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 4), teach that the gopis' *parakiya-rasa* is the purest prema, wholly unlike worldly passion; Lochana Dasa himself says that by this prema the Lord is conquered.
[^07-2]: The words of Uddhava. In the edition the verse is followed by Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya and translation: "Oh! The gopis in Vrindavana, abandoning kinsmen hard to abandon — husbands, fathers-in-law and others — and the path of dharma, served the lotus feet of Mukunda, which the Vedas seek. Shall I obtain any birth at all in Vrindavana among the bushes, creepers and herbs that serve the dust of the gopis' feet?"
[^07-3]: An echo of the *Bhagavata* (10.47.59), where Uddhava says: "Where are these forest women, tainted by infidelity, and where is this love for Krishna, the Supreme Self!" — to show that such love is given irrespective of merit. The words "unchaste", "lustful" express the worldly view; in the Gaudiya understanding the *kama* of the gopis is pure prema, whose only desire is Krishna's joy (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 4.164–171).
[^07-4]: The raga and disha are given after the 1983 edition; in A the line is illegible.
[^07-5]: A has the past tense ("has taken birth"); the 1983 edition the future ("will take birth"), as also in v. 474.
[^07-6]: The 1983 edition has four couplets here: people have abandoned charity, vows and austerity and toil only for the belly; brahmanas have abandoned the worship of Krishna, and kshatriyas, vaishyas and shudras the service of brahmanas; abandoning respect for father and mother, all honour their wives with body, speech and mind. A has a single couplet (from the first and last lines).
[^07-7]: *Daru-brahman*, "Brahman in wood": the Lord Himself, manifest in the wooden Deity of Jagannatha at Puri (Nilachala).
[^07-8]: See vv. 257–271: the Lord promised Katyayani (Parvati) to fulfil her vow concerning mahaprasada and to appear in Kali as Gaura; Narada then proclaimed this at His command.
[^07-9]: So Lochana Dasa: in Goloka, the highest abode above Vaikuntha, Gaurasundara Himself is king. In the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding (Bhaktivinoda Thakura, Bhaktisiddhanta) Gaura's abode, Navadvipa-dhama, is non-different from Goloka-Vrindavana and is part of it; Gaura is Krishna Himself with the bhava and lustre of Radha.
[^07-10]: Lochana Dasa sets side by side Radha (source of the gopis) and Rukmini (source of the queens of Dvaraka). The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 4.75–81), teach that Radha is the original *hladini-shakti* and the source of all the Lord's beloveds: the Lakshmis of Vaikuntha, the queens of Dvaraka and the gopis.
[^07-11]: The subject of the stanza is not named; probably the description of Satyabhama as the embodiment of music continues — or the deity of music is introduced as another dweller of Goloka.
[^07-12]: Perhaps "Ranga" ("Play") is the name of the city; it may also be understood as "the city full of rasa and play".
[^07-13]: *Maya* here is His energy, not an illusion that rules Him; the "reflections" (*chhaya*) are His expansions. The expression is Lochana Dasa's own; in Gaudiya theology all avataras are portions and parts (*amsha, kala*) of Krishna (*Bhag.* 1.3.28). In the 1983 edition the second half of the stanza reads: "…hundreds of avataras — He alone is the merciful King".
[^07-14]: This stanza (like the previous one) differs in the 1983 edition: "I, Vishnu, am His reflection (*chhaya*), Lakshmi the reflection of [His] wealth, this Vaikuntha the reflection of [His] Vaikuntha"; we follow A ("portion"). The thought agrees with Gaudiya teaching: Narayana of Vaikuntha is a *vilasa* expansion of Krishna, and Vaikuntha an expansion of Goloka (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 5). Line 3 is obscure; the 1983 edition has "all, established in bhakti, serve…".
[^07-15]: Lines 3–4 are obscure; perhaps the "three qualities of shakti" are the Lord's three energies: *hladini*, *sandhini* and *samvit* (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 4.62). In the 1983 edition line 3 differs: "prakriti on the right and left, named Lalita and Vishakha".
[^07-16]: Kama (Madana) is the god of love. Here not the worldly Cupid is meant: the spiritual beauty of Goloka is described, where, in the words of the Gaudiya scriptures, Krishna (and Gaura) is *aprakrita-Madana*, the transcendental Cupid who enchants Cupid himself.
[^07-17]: The bathing (*abhisheka*) of Gaura in Goloka. Among those taking part are queens of Dvaraka (Rukmini, Satyabhama, Nagnajiti, Mitravinda, Lakshmana-Sulakshana, Bhadra), Radhika and companions from Vraja (Chandramukhi, Madhupriya), and Tilottama; Lochana Dasa unites the consorts of Dvaraka and Vraja in one vision.
[^07-18]: Which four-syllable mantra is meant the author does not say.

---

# Sutra-khanda, part 8 (portion 08)

*Narada glorifies Gaura, who sends him to Balarama in Shvetadvipa; the Lord reveals to His associates why He will be born on earth; Narada with Balarama, who on hearing the command bids His own to take birth on earth; the descent of the associates — Advaita, Nityananda, Sita and many others; praise of Narahari Sarakara, Raghunandana and Mukunda Dasa; the author's humility; the end of the Sutra-khanda*

**531.** The sage says: "Mahaprabhu, such a wonder I have never
seen nor heard of.
Today my life has borne fruit — I have seen a treasury of nectar,
and I count myself blessed, blessed.

**532.** Brahma and the others do not know the truth; Your coming is unknown [to them];
I call You inconceivable.
Some call [You] effulgence — but even they cannot express [You] in words:
there is no comparison to tell it.

**533.** Some say, 'Higher than the highest, the chief, the best Purusha' —
but by reasoning they cannot determine [You].
Seeing that Your shakti is in all, they do not attain liberation;
Your deeds are beyond reach.

**534.** Thousand-headed Ananta, finding no end to [Your] virtues,
has taken two tongues in every mouth;
finding no limit to Your virtues — such a Lord are You, Gaura —
by good fortune I have seen You!

**535.** And those who long [for You], following Your feet,
are of various understanding, not of one opinion:
some say, 'The All-pervading' — these are the Sankhya-yogis who speak of the subtle;
the devotees serve [Your] visible form.

**536.** Others, following the Vedas, perform the eternal dharma and rites,
adhering to the dharma of varna and ashrama;
and the conclusion of Vedanta I do not find [with them]:
not understanding, they say many things.

**537.** Why there is contradiction among them they do not consider,
and again they say, 'All is one, non-dual'.
Not understanding Your heart, each holding to his side, they perform their rites —
Your word is unknown to all.

**538.** Now by the grace of [Your] feet my heart weeps unceasingly:
leaving this perishable form,
may I be born again, make Krishna my whole world
and practise this prema-bhakti."[^08-1]

**539.** Hearing such words of Narada, the Jewel of virtues says:
"Go, go, go, king of sages!
I shall deliver the people of Kali, spread devotion to Myself,
I shall be born among the people of Nadia.

**540.** Go [down] to the earth: in Shvetadvipa I abide
[in the form of] a brother named Balarama;
Ananta is His portion, the eleven Rudras are His line,
Lord Mahesha serves Him.[^08-2]

**541.** With His beloved Revati He dwells in joyful play
amid the land in the Ocean of Milk;
whatever avataras there are, He alone is their helper;
in every work of Mine I place Him first.

**542.** Go, go, king of sages, make this task known to Him,
tell Him all in due order:
'Taking all Your portions, go and take birth on earth,
and bear the name Nityananda.'"

**543.** In bliss Narada Muni, hearing the Lord's words,
cries "Haribol!" from the joy of his heart.
Says Lochana Dasa: whoever hears the conversation of these two,
in him a wave of bliss arises.

*Raga dhanashi, short metre; disha: "I give myself to the crimson lotus feet! Go, go, give out prema, madden the world with prema!"*

**544.** Having sent Narada off, the Lord sat down,
and the words of His heart sprang up like a shoot.

**545.** For what reason He will be born on earth —
I shall tell the essence; listen, all, attentively.

**546.** Gathering His own, the Lord speaks of Himself:
the Supreme Lord of lords is thinking of the earth.

**547.** On the right is Radhika, on the left the goddess Sri Rukmini,
nearest to them all the chief companions of play,

**548.** after them all the dear associates,
after them all the other followers.

**549.** Longing to hear the dear words of the Lord of their life,
hundreds of thousands of eyes [are fixed] on one beautiful face:

**550.** like many chakoras hoping for one moon,
they drink heaps of nectar from the shining face.

**551.** "Yuga after yuga I take birth on earth
to deliver the righteous and to protect dharma.[^08-3]

**552.** I establish dharma — but no one understands,
and sin grows ever more: what a calamity!

**553.** More than in the Satya-yuga sin grows in the Treta,
in the Dvapara still more: great is the sorrow!

**554.** Kali is pitch darkness, not a trace of dharma is there;
seeing the sufferings of all, I am filled with compassion.

**555.** My descent is for the destruction of adharma;
if adharma grows again, what then is My work?

**556.** Knowing this, compassion arose in My heart:
I shall take birth to reveal My prema.

**557.** Revealing such rare prema-bhakti,
I shall make people understand, discerning dharma from adharma.

**558.** I shall be born in Navadvipa, of Shachi,
near the Ganga, in the house of Jagannatha Mishra.

**559.** The other avataras are not like this avatara:
they come to earth to slay the asuras.

**560.** Huge great asuras, with My great weapons,
in great battles I struck down and crushed.

**561.** But now all people are asuras in their hearts:
they cannot be cut by a sword — what can be done by force of arms?

**562.** The name, the virtues, sankirtana — this is the Vaishnava's power;
and further I shall reveal prema-bhakti for Myself.

**563.** In this way I shall destroy the sin of Kali;
come, all, do not consider who goes before and who after.

**564.** Now, taking the sharp sword of the sankirtana of the name,
I shall cut down the asuras within the hearts of living beings.

**565.** If a sinner, abandoning dharma, goes to a distant land,
My generals, the devotees, will go there.

**566.** With My prema I shall flood this whole universe
and not leave a single drop of grief and sorrow.

**567.** I shall flood the moving and the unmoving, and the gods."
Hearing this, joyfully speaks this servant Lochana.

*Raga varadi, tripadi metre*

**568.** Narada Muni set out, the sound of his vina arose;
his hands and feet no longer obey him;
he walks but does not see the way — tears of prema flood his eyes —
he staggers as if drunk.

**569.** He goes two or four steps and falls again on the same spot,
half uttering the name of the Lord;
with great effort he rises, clutching at the ground,[^08-4]
and a river of tears flows from his eyes.

**570.** Now great madness, cries of "hum!", a lion's roar,
in his heart meditation on Gora's form;
he has no outer awareness, does not tell himself from others —
in all there is only the awareness of Gaura.

**571.** Like the splendour of millions of suns — such is the radiance of his body;
Narada flies through the sky
and descended at that abode where Lord Balarama is —
Shvetadvipa was lit up.

**572.** Stopping at the city's bounds, he looks in amazement on every side:
the radiance of hundreds of thousands of moons;
a soft, soft breeze blows, [bearing] the divine fragrance of flowers,
pearls hang at every gate.

**573.** All the people there are in sattva; there is no old age, death or sorrow;
everyone is the friend of all;
wherever one looks, all the people are lovely;
the Ocean of Milk is full of Baladeva.

**574.** Seeing this, Narada Muni says in his mind, "Blessed, blessed!"
and counts himself blessed, blessed:
"The Lord of the three worlds, the Master, I shall see with my eyes
and fall weeping at His feet.

**575.** This Lord Balarama is the helper yuga after yuga:
making Him [His helper], Krishna descends;
playing games of every kind, in endless joyful lilas
He destroys the asuras.

**576.** This Lord Balarama, by His portions in three abodes,[^08-5]
abides and serves Krishna with love;
at the beginning, the middle and the end — His portion is Ananta,
who holds the earth upon one hood.

**577.** Himself being the Lord, dwelling in Shvetadvipa,
He enjoys pastimes in many ways;
His highest fulfilment is service
to that Mahaprabhu, in wondrous ways.

**578.** When [the Lord] walks he is the umbrella, when He sits the seat and garments,
when He lies down the bed;
at the dissolution the banyan leaf, in great battle the divine weapon:
in many forms he serves.

**579.** With one portion He serves, with another portion He holds the earth —
such is my Lord Balarama.
The supreme King of the three worlds I shall see amid the Ocean of Milk
and convey to Him the Lord's command."

**580.** These two Lords are like a king and his chief minister:
with one counsel they protect the earth;
and all the others of the Rudras' line — they too, portions of His portions —
will descend to earth.

**581.** Absorbed in the rasa of these thoughts, the sage, beside himself,
entered the city in great bliss
and beheld the Lord of the three worlds with all His associates —
the wondrous moon-like Balarama.

**582.** Like a mountain of nectar He sits on a white throne,[^08-6]
smiling softly, sweet as nectar;
His eyes red lotuses, seen drooping languidly,
half-words come from His lips.

**583.** The pupils, like bees, are half covered [by the lids],
both eyes half drowsy;
gems, pearls, coral, divine necklaces of jewels —
the limbs cannot be seen for the ornaments.

**584.** Leaning idly on a pillow, his left hand under his head,
with his right he holds Revati's hand;
Revati, betel in hand, puts it to his lips
and gazes lovingly at his face.

**585.** Maidservants on every side, smiling, wave chamaras;
the tinkling of bracelets and girdle-bells is heard;
some play the vina and the flute, some sing,
the loveliest women keep the beat.

**586.** Beyond them are hundreds and hundreds of followers,
each at his appointed service.
At that moment the sage sounded his vina,
and the Lord suddenly saw him.

**587.** Overwhelmed, Narada Muni staggers and falls to the ground;
the Lord lifted him and took him on His lap,
and, with long-held love, looked on that great fortunate one
and comforted him with cool, great words.

**588.** Greeting him with a smile, the Lord says: "Where have you come from?
I feel you will tell [Me] a secret.
Tell Me, what is the task? As I listen, within My heart
bliss rises beyond counting."

**589.** Respectfully the sage says: "What can I know how to say?
You, Lord, are the Indweller of all.
What I can say, I shall say as I understand;
do what is fitting, Lord.

**590.** 'In the sinful Kali-yuga I see no deliverance for people' —
compassion arose in the Lord's heart —
'to protect the devotees and establish dharma
I shall be born on earth.

**591.** For the destruction of adharma, what other dharma is there?' —
so I understood from His bearing and signs.
He commanded me to make the proclamation,
and on hearing it people rejoiced.

**592.** With the bhava of Radha within and the colour of Radha without,
filled with Radha inside and out,
with His friends and girl-companions and endless devotees
He will madden the whole world with the bhava of Vraja.[^08-7]

**593.** 'With limbs, sub-limbs and associates take birth on earth
and bear the name Nityananda' —
the heart of His deeds and form is not unknown to You —
this is the command that Gaurachandra spoke."

**594.** Hearing this, Lord Balarama looks around in bliss
and laughs aloud with great peals;
again and again He roars "hum!", displays wonders,
forgets Himself in the bliss of prema.

**595.** He commanded His own: "Go to the earth
to carry out the Lord's command.
Go, Narada: I shall take birth
and make the unseen seen."

**596.** Such a wondrous tale, the story of Gaura's virtues — hear it,
all of you, be attentive:
the essence of all avataras is Gora, the avatara of Kali —
consider this, all, in your minds.

**597.** Holding a straw between my teeth, with a contrite heart I say:
do not neglect Gora's virtues;
do not give your mind to worldly life, love Krishna —
this is the raft for crossing the ocean of worldly existence.

**598.** What has never been — that Gora-avatara
will be fully manifest;
the lifeless will gain life, the blind will discern the path —
so sings His virtues this Lochana Dasa.

*Raga bhatiyari; disha: "Brothers, sing, sing the tale of the virtues of Nitai and Chaitanya!"*

**599.** When Mahaprabhu had so commanded,
all took birth by their portions.

**600.** Mahesha Thakura went forth before all:
He was born in a brahmana family, named Kamalaksha.

**601.** By study and hearing He became accomplished in virtue
and received the title "Advaita Acharya".[^08-8]

**602.** This great Maheshvara is endowed with sattva —
yet the world proclaims Him [the embodiment of] tamo-guna.

**603.** No one considers what He is within and without:
seeing His outer conduct, they say "tamo-guna".

**604.** [Shiva] is Krishna's very self; their name is Hari-Hara;
the worldly tamo-guna is set aside among his qualities.[^08-9]

**605.** Whoever, taking him for a worldly devotee, calls him "tamoguni" —
when I know of him, I shall call him low, a petty man.

**606.** How can you say that Hari-Hara is tamo-guna?
Show no disrespect, if you listen to my words.

**607.** Reflect in your minds and consider:
this is why I say that Gora is the essence of all avataras.

**608.** In all the avataras the companion of His play,
Balarama, took birth on this earth —

**609.** in a brahmana family, as befits the dharma of the yuga:
the eternal (*nitya*) root of bliss (*ananda*) is His innate form.

**610.** One portion of Him bears a thousand hoods,
and on one hood holds the earth to preserve creation.

**611.** Balarama was born of Padmavati;
His father was Hado Ojha, by name also Paramananda.[^08-10]

**612.** His father and mother named Him Kuvera Pandita;
in the ashrama of sannyasa He is Nityananda of wondrous deeds.[^08-11]

**613.** Under an auspicious conjunction, on the bright thirteenth of the month of Magha,
He took birth on earth in great joy.

**614.** Katyayani took birth on earth
and bears the name Sita in the society of brahmanas.

**615.** She lives together with Advaita Thakura,
and the two together reveal prema-bhakti.

**616.** I am small of mind — what do I know of who is what?
How can I expound whose avatara anyone is?

**617.** Even what I have heard with my ears from the mouths of mahantas
I cannot repeat: my heart shrinks back.

**618.** I have no power to determine it —
I only name those who are.

**619.** Let no one consider in his mind who is named before and who after:
for the sake of the metre the book keeps no order [of rank].

**620.** Shachi-devi and Jagannatha Mishra Purandara,
in whose house the Lord Himself took birth;

**621.** Gopinatha by name, and Kashi Mishra Thakura,[^08-12]
on the path approved by Chaitanya, in abundant bliss;

**622.** Pandita Sri Gadadhara, Gadadhara Dasa,
Murari, Mukunda Datta and Srinivasa;

**623.** Raya Ramananda and Vasudeva Datta,
Haridasa Thakura and Govinda the attendant;

**624.** Ishvara [Puri], Madhava Puri and Vishnupuri,
Vakreshvara, Paramananda Puri of pure conduct;

**625.** Pandita Jagadananda and Vishnupriya,
Raghava Pandita and others came to earth;

**626.** Ramadasa, Gauridasa and Sundara,
Krishnadasa, Purushottama, Sri Kamalakara,

**627.** Kala Krishnadasa and Uddharana Datta —
the twelve cowherd boys (*gopalas*) of Vraja: such is their greatness;[^08-13]

**628.** Parameshvara Dasa and Vrindavana Dasa,
Kashishvara, Srila Rupa and Sanatana appeared;

**629.** Govinda, Madhava Ghosha and Vasu Ghosha —
all came together and spread [bhakti] over the earth.

**630.** Together with Damodara Pandita, five brothers
took birth on earth in one place.[^08-14]

**631.** Purandara Pandita and Paramananda Vaidya —
all who were, from the first to the last, came to earth.

**632.** Sri Narahari Dasa is my Thakura:
of his deeds I shall say something in particular.

**633.** What can I know how to tell of his deeds?
[I tell] only what I grasp by the power of my mind.

**634.** Let no one harbour any resentment:
I bow at the feet of my own guru,

**635.** by the grace of whose feet I, such a wretch,
tell you all of the Lord's virtues.

**636.** Sri Narahari Dasa is my Thakura,
whose power is the glory of the vaidya lineage.

**637.** Unchecked is his love for Krishna, his body is full of Krishna;
to his followers he teaches nothing but prema.

**638.** His heart aches with compassion for countless beings,
his soul is ever restless with love for Krishna.

**639.** His body seems moulded of the rasa of Radha and Krishna:
bhavas rise in him, now this, now that.

**640.** In the rasa of Radha and Krishna lies his pure fame;[^08-15]
he dwells in the land of Shrikhanda.

**641.** "Narahari of Chaitanya" — so this master is renowned;
apart from his feet I have no other refuge.

**642.** Now Krishna, now Radha — rapt in their bhavas,
he displays the rasa of Radha and Krishna embodied.

**643.** On the path approved by Chaitanya his judgement is pure;
peerless and full of rasa are his bhavas, [as of] all the avataras.

**644.** To all Vaishnavas due honour and love;
through all the world his pure fame.

**645.** In Vrindavana he was named Madhumati,
a dear companion of Radha, a treasury of sweetness.[^08-16]

**646.** Now, in the Kali age, Narahari, companion of Gaura,
is the keeper of the treasury of the prema of Radha and Krishna.

**647.** His nephew, Sri Raghunandana Thakura,
is widely proclaimed throughout the world.

**648.** He who fed the Sri Murti a sweet ball (*laddu*) —[^08-17]
what fool would think him of little understanding?

**649.** A Vaishnava by nature is beyond the bounds of varna;
he who converses with Krishna has Krishna alone.[^08-18]

**650.** He whose constant vow is converse with the Sri Murti —
how can you know what he is, what his greatness is?

**651.** He to whom Chaitanya said, "You are My life",
whom Abhirama Gosvami revealed,

**652.** making known that he was the avatara of Madana —
and all saw him so on Chaitanya's lap.[^08-19]

**653.** When he dances absorbed in Krishna he enchants the world's heart;
he has no "mine" and "another's" — the same love for all.

**654.** Always sweet words come from his lips;
no one ever heard a harsh word from him.

**655.** Skill, sweetness, lila, play, loveliness —
his body, full of rasa, is blessed in this world.

**656.** His father is the wise Sri Mukunda Dasa,
pure in faith on the path approved by Chaitanya.

**657.** Seeing a peacock feather in the king's presence,
he fell senseless, the form of Krishna drawing his mind.[^08-20]

**658.** Who knows what the rasa of Chaitanya's companions is?
Ananta and the others know it — His limbs and companions.

**659.** Can [an ordinary] being see the glory of Krishna?
He alone sees in whom there is realization of Krishna.

**660.** What more shall I say? All His warriors and associates
came to earth — how many shall I name?

**661.** If I could measure the water of the ocean with a pitcher,
if I could count the dust of the earth grain by grain,

**662.** if I could count the stars of the sky —
still I could not describe the avatara of Gora.

**663.** I am very small of mind — what more shall I say?
A fool, I venture to reason on the Vedas.

**664.** As a blind man without sight wants to see a divine jewel,
as a dwarf stretches out his arms to catch the moon,

**665.** as a lame man boasts that he will stride across the earth,
as a tiny ant wants to carry a mountain —

**666.** such a desire has arisen in my heart:
to proclaim the tale of the avatara of Gora.

**667.** With joined palms I say: listen, all!
Gora makes this mute one eloquent.

**668.** The tongueless speaks clear and sweet words,
the unlettered fool tells the tale of Brahman.

**669.** Great, great devotees, born on earth,
make manifest the secret words of Krishna.

**670.** Without cause they are merciful to all beings,
as a mother nurses a wayward son.

**671.** Seeing such boundless mercy of the Lord,
I, though low, long for nectar.

**672.** Sri Narahari Dasa, whose body is mercy itself,
seeing a sinner, has boundless mercy and love.

**673.** Me, an incorrigible sinner, blind, of evil conduct,
seeing me helpless, he showed mercy.

**674.** By the power of his mercy and by the grace of the Vaishnavas —
in this I trust — the book will be completed without hindrance.

**675.** With joined palms I say in a humble voice:
I give myself to the feet of the Vaishnavas.

**676.** There is no one on earth lower than I;
by the power of the Vaishnavas' mercy may the work succeed.

**677.** Holding a straw between his teeth, this Lochana Dasa
bows and entreats: fulfil my hope!

**678.** The book of the Sutra-khanda is ended — listen, all:
now in the Adi-khanda I shall tell of the descent.

**679.** The telling of the "sutra" is ended; now come the pastimes of prema —
with joy in his heart says this Lochana Dasa.

*Thus ends the Sutra-khanda of the* Sri Chaitanya-mangala *composed by Sri Lochana Dasa Thakura.*

[^08-1]: Narada, the Lord's eternal associate, speaks thus out of humility, as a devotee who longs for service.
[^08-2]: In the 1983 edition line 1 reads: "Go, Narada: in Shvetadvipa I abide." Balarama is the Lord's second body; according to Gaudiya teaching, from Him (as Sankarshana) come Ananta-Shesha, and Shiva with the Rudras (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 5).
[^08-3]: Cf. *Bhagavad-gita* 4.8.
[^08-4]: The line is damaged in A ("ধরিয়া ব্রণী-কোডি"); after the 1983 edition, "leaning on the earth".
[^08-5]: Probably the three abodes of the Purusha-avataras — Karanodakashayi, Garbhodakashayi and Kshirodakashayi Vishnu — who, according to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 5), are expansions of Sankarshana-Balarama.
[^08-6]: "Mountain of nectar" after the 1903 edition ("অমৃত-পর্বত"); in A and the 1983 edition the first word is corrupt.
[^08-7]: Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 4.55 ff.): Krishna appears as Gaura, taking the bhava and lustre of Radha, to taste Her love.
[^08-8]: Lochana Dasa calls Advaita an avatara of Mahesha (Shiva). According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 6.4–27) Advaita is Maha-Vishnu and also Sada-Shiva; Bhaktisiddhanta in the summary of this section says: "Shiva [appears] as Advaita Prabhu."
[^08-9]: The line admits two readings: *parakrita* (Sanskrit "rejected, set aside") — tamo-guna is set aside in him; or *prakrita* ("worldly") — the worldly tamo-guna [appears only] among his outward qualities. The thought is the same in either case: Shiva (and Advaita) is not under the sway of tamas; cf. *Bhag.* 12.13.16: "Shambhu is the best of Vaishnavas."
[^08-10]: Nityananda's father is usually called Hadai Pandita (Hadai Ojha), and His mother Padmavati. The name "Paramananda" is Lochana Dasa's.
[^08-11]: The 1983 edition has "with renunciation in His heart — Nityananda of wondrous deeds". The name "Kuvera Pandita" is found only in Lochana Dasa.
[^08-12]: Probably Gopinatha Acharya, Sarvabhauma's brother-in-law, and Kashi Mishra, in whose house the Lord lived at Puri; the line can also be read as one person: "Kashi Mishra Thakura, named Gopinatha".
[^08-13]: Cf. the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 11) and the *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*: the twelve gopalas are cowherd boys of Vraja who appeared as associates of Nityananda (among them Abhirama, Sundarananda, Gauridasa Pandita, Kala Krishnadasa, Purushottama, Kamalakara, Uddharana Datta, Parameshvara Dasa and others).
[^08-14]: The sense is unclear: perhaps Damodara Pandita and his brothers (so in A and in the 1983 edition).
[^08-15]: In A the beginning of the line is corrupt ("ক্ষণে…"); in the 1983 edition stanzas 640–644 are in a different order and lack the second line of v. 641.
[^08-16]: So also the *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika* (177): Narahari Sarakara is Madhumati, a companion of Radha in Vraja.
[^08-17]: A well-known tradition: the boy Raghunandana, entrusted by his father with the worship, persuaded the Deity of Gopinatha at Shrikhanda to eat a laddu, and the Deity ate it.
[^08-18]: The line also admits the reading "he is Krishna himself"; we follow the understanding consistent with Gaudiya teaching: the devotee does not become Krishna, but for him there is Krishna alone.
[^08-19]: Abhirama Thakura (Ramadasa), one of the twelve gopalas, is said to have recognized the Lord's eternal associates in people. "Madana" is Kamadeva; according to the *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika* Raghunandana is Pradyumna (Kandarpa) of Dvaraka.
[^08-20]: Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 15.121–125): Mukunda Dasa, a court physician, on seeing a fan of peacock feathers above the king's head, remembered Krishna and fell senseless.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 1 (portion 09)

*The beginning of the Adi-khanda: the Lord enters Shachi's womb; Advaita Acharya worships Shachi's womb; the praises of the gods; Gaura's appearance on the full moon of Phalguna during an eclipse; the infant's form and marks; the rapture of the people of Nadia; the name Nimai, the festivals of the eighth and ninth days; the beginning of the Balya-lila*

## Janma-lila (The Birth)

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* In this khanda first of all the appearance on earth of the transcendental Sri Gaurahari is described. He who is the original cause of all causes, Parabrahman Narayana, appeared in the womb of Shachi. As her womb grew day by day, so day by day the lustre of her body increased. Seeing the wondrous lustre of her body, all were amazed and concluded: "Surely some great personage has appeared in Shachi's womb." When six months were complete, one day Advaita Acharya Prabhu came to the house of Shachi and Jagannatha, bowed to Shachi's womb and circumambulated it; the reason for this neither Shachi nor Jagannatha could then understand. On some days Shachi-devi saw Brahma, Shiva and the other gods come before her womb, offer praise and pray to the Lord, who gives prema even to chandalas, for the love never given before; and in bliss her heart was filled with compassion for all beings. Gradually ten months were completed. Then, on the full moon of Phalguna, on the pretext of an eclipse, accompanied by the sankirtana of Hari's name, Bhagavan Gaurachandra descended to earth from the ocean of Shachi's womb; the ten directions were filled with bliss, and gods and goddesses, men and women, all longing to see the moon-like face of Shachi's son, came to Shachi's house; at that time her house became Vaikuntha. Jagannatha Mishra and the men and women of Nadia (who had come to see) were all amazed to see on the lotus feet of the child — with a lion's neck, an elephant's shoulders and a broad chest — the marks of the flag, the thunderbolt and the goad, and various superhuman marks on His other limbs; all concluded that this child was surely not human. Then are described the distribution of the eight kinds of pulses on the eighth day, the festival on the ninth, and the one-pointed love of the neighbours, men and women, for [Shachi's] son.

*Raga dhanashi; disha: "Glory to Narahari and Gadadhara, the Lords of my life! Cast, O Lord, an auspicious glance upon living beings! Lord Gorachand — glory, glory!"*[^09-1]

**1.** Glory, glory to Gadadhara, Gauranga and Narahari!
Glory, glory to Nityananda, the bearer of all energies!

**2.** Glory, glory to Advaita Acharya, Maheshvara!
Glory, glory to the great devotees of Gauranga!

**3.** Placing on my head the dust of the feet of all,
I tell the story of the Adi-khanda — listen attentively.

**4.** When all His own had taken birth,
the cry rang out: "Make ready, make ready!"

**5.** "I go to earth, there must be no more delay" —
and the Lord Himself took shelter in Shachi's womb.

**6.** Cries of "Jaya, jaya!" filled the universe;
gods, nagas and men look on, overcome with prema.

**7.** He whom some call the eternal Effulgence,
whom others call Narayana, subtle and gross,

**8.** whom others call Parabrahman, gross and subtle —
He took shelter in Shachi's womb.

**9.** Radiant, airlike,[^09-2] the burden of the womb grows daily;
seeing it, the love of all people grows.

**10.** One, two, three, four, five, six months —
in Shachi's womb great bliss reveals itself.

**11.** Day by day the radiance of Shachi's body grows;
seeing it, all the people rejoice at heart.

**12.** "We do not know who has come into Shachi's house" —
so in every house all are pondering.

**13.** When six months of Shachi's womb were complete,
the house shone with the splendour of her body.

**14.** At that time a wondrous thing took place:
suddenly Advaita Acharya came there.

**15.** In the house sat Jagannatha, best of the twice-born;
seeing Advaita Acharya, he rose respectfully.

**16.** Advaita Acharya Gosani is the abode of all virtues,
blessed in the three worlds, without equal.

**17.** Seeing Him, Mishra Purandara with great respect
himself brought a seat for Him to sit on,

**18.** and placed the dust of His feet on his head;
the Acharya, abashed, showed much humility.

**19.** Shachi-devi gave water to wash His feet;
seeing Shachi, He, full of love, rose respectfully.

**20.** With love His two lotus eyes grew red,
His eyes brimmed with tears, His face was flushed,

**21.** His lips trembled, His voice faltered;
He could not hold His body steady — He staggered.

**22.** Circumambulating Shachi, He bows to her;
Shachi-devi started, seeing this breach of custom.[^09-3]

**23.** Jagannatha is perplexed, Shachi amazed:
"What are you doing, what are you doing?" she says, pained at heart.

**24.** Jagannatha says: "Listen, Acharya Gosani:
no one can understand Your conduct.

**25.** Be kind and tell us, dispel our doubt,
or else the fire of this anxiety will burn my body."

**26.** The Acharya said: "Listen, Mishra Purandara:
later you will know everything" — such was His answer.

**27.** His whole body thrilled: knowing the matter,
He anointed Shachi's holy womb with fragrant sandal,

**28.** circumambulated [her] seven times, bowed down,
and without saying anything went to His own place.

**29.** Here Shachi and Jagannatha ponder:
"Why did He worship my womb?"

**30.** Acharya Gosani worshipped the womb —
and Shachi's radiance grew a hundredfold; she forgets herself.

**31.** She sees all as joy, sees no sorrow;
she sees all the gods before her.

**32.** Brahma, Shiva, Sanaka and the others — all the gods,
facing her womb, offer praise:

**33.** "Glory, glory to the Infinite, the Non-dual, the Eternal!
Glory to the Bliss of Achyuta, the Eternal Bliss, Janardana![^09-4]

**34.** Glory to Him who is beyond prakriti — sattva, rajas and tamas!
Glory to Maha-Vishnu [abiding] in the Causal Ocean!

**35.** Glory to the Lord of Paravyoma, whose greatness is boundless!
Glory to [Him who is] sattva, the highest sattva, whose form is pure sattva!

**36.** Glory to the Lord of Goloka, the beloved of Radha!
Glory, glory to the supreme Lord of the countless Vaikunthas!

**37.** Glory, glory to the carefree, playful hero (*dhira-lalita*)!
Glory, glory to the all-enchanting son of Nanda!

**38.** Now, in the Kali-yuga, appearing in Shachi's womb,
You have come to taste Your own play Yourself.

**39.** Glory, glory to this Lord, the giver of supreme bliss!
Such mercy has never been.

**40.** In the age of Kali You have become the giver of Yourself:
with Gadadhara there will be no judging of worthy and unworthy.[^09-5]

**41.** That prema for which we, all the gods, beg
we have not obtained — not a particle, not even its fragrance.

**42.** That prema, that sweet rasa, You will taste Yourself
and give to all to taste, down to the chandala, not looking at faults.

**43.** May we too obtain a particle of Your prema,
may we sing with You the virtues of Radha and Krishna.

**44.** Glory, glory to Gaurahari, the giver of sankirtana!"
So saying, the gods circumambulate [her].

**45.** With four mouths Brahma offers many kinds of praise;
Shachi-devi grew afraid, her mind startled.

**46.** Shachi's heart was filled with compassion for all beings:
she is kind [to all] as to herself, not distinguishing her own from others.

**47.** Ten months of the pregnancy were complete; [joy] on every side —
the goddess forgets herself with the joy of her heart.

**48.** An auspicious day, an auspicious hour, the lunar day of the full moon,
the auspicious night of Phalguna, the radiance of the moon.

**49.** At a wondrous hour Rahu swallows the moon —
and on every side arose the cry: "Hari, Hari!"[^09-6]

**50.** All around was filled with a divine, lovely fragrance;
the ten directions grew bright, the breeze blew soft and gentle.

**51.** At that time all six seasons appeared at once —
at such an hour the Lord took His auspicious birth on earth.

**52.** In the sky the gods look on from their celestial cars,
running in love to see the body of Gaura.

**53.** Only one sound is heard: "Haribol, Haribol!" —
this my Lord revealed the moment He was born.

**54.** In Shachi's house the riches of Vaikuntha appeared;[^09-7]
beside herself with bliss, Shachi speaks in a faltering voice;

**55.** with a gesture she calls Jagannatha Pandita:
"Our life has borne fruit — look at our son's face!"

**56.** The women of the town joyfully cry "Jaya, jaya!",
all beside themselves with bliss at the sight of the child.

**57.** The wives of sages, goddesses and naga maidens all came —
seeing Gauranga, they raised the cry "Jaya, jaya!"

**58.** The fragrance of Gaura's majesty filled the universe;
each of His limbs is a treasury of rasa, unbroken nectar.

**59.** Gazing and gazing, all their eyes were soothed;
to all it seemed: this is the life of the nagaris of Vraja.

**60.** "Such a child we have never seen or heard of;
looking at Him, the heart does — we know not what!

**61.** Such marks we have never seen in a human being,"
say the celestial beauties; "later we shall know [who He is]."

**62.** Jagannatha is beside himself, looking at his son's face:
the universe cannot contain the delight of his heart.

**63.** How many moons are rising — so looks His little face!
His face is likened to a blossoming lotus.

**64.** His high nose surpasses the sesame flower,
the radiance of Gora's body sparkles — nectar.

**65.** His lips are red, His cheeks lovely and shining;
seeing His beautiful chin, love wells up.

**66.** A lion's neck, an elephant's shoulders, a broad chest,
arms reaching to the knees, a body full of rasa.

**67.** Broad hips, thighs like plantain stems,
two feet like petals of a red lotus.

**68.** On the lotus soles — the flag, the thunderbolt, the goad,
the chariot, the umbrella, the chamara, the svastika, the jambu fruit,

**69.** the upward line, the triangle, the elephant's kumbha[^09-8] —
this whole wondrous form pours out nectar.

**70.** In such a wondrous form upon the earth
shine marks surpassing [those of] great kings.

**71.** Indra, Chandra, gandharvas, kinnaras and gods
came to earth — what a joyous wonder!

**72.** The eyes of all seemed anointed with a salve of nectar —
as if after long love they saw their beloved.

**73.** The child was just born — yet to those who saw Him
it seemed He had long been their friend of old.

**74.** From each limb He pours nectar in heaps;
why, when the eyes behold [Him], does the heart [so stir]?

**75.** Seeing the child, [the women's] bosoms filled with joy;
why are their eyes languid, why have the knots of their garments loosened?[^09-9]

**76.** The moment the newborn child was seen,
His beautiful face surpassed millions of Kamas.

**77.** So reflecting, all cry "Jaya, jaya!
Shachi's son by His very nature is not human.

**78.** Shachi's son is a new Kamadeva:
even His crying is nectar to the ears."

**79.** "The Lord of Goloka Himself has descended" —
such was the conclusion the women reached.

**80.** The Lord of all the worlds has appeared on earth —
beside himself with bliss says this Lochana Dasa.

*Raga mangala-gurjari; disha: "Rejoice, celebrate! In bliss, in Shachi's house, sing of Gora's virtues!"*

*Mishra Purandara, overflowing with bliss —
his voice faltering —
summoning his kin and friends without delay,
celebrates the festival of his son's birth.*[^09-10]

**81.** "Jaya, jaya, jaya!" — all around is full of joy,
the town is filled with bliss;
all the wives of good families came by hundreds
and distribute sindura and pithali.[^09-11]

**82.** Taking her son on her lap, in bliss, full of love,
Shachi-devi speaks in a faltering voice:
"Give your blessing, give the dust of your feet as a boon —
may the boy live long!

**83.** The boy is not mine — call Him your own and bless Him,
all you women!"
His body is sweeter than nectar; to ward off an evil outcome
they gave Him the name Nimai.[^09-12]

**84.** On this eighth day, for the children's delight,
the "eight kinds of pulses" are given out;
the festival of the ninth night — all is bliss,
joyful music plays.[^09-13]

**85.** Day by day Shachi's son grows —
a full moon upon the earth;
His two eyes bright with kajal,
a gorochana tilaka beautifully drawn.

**86.** Again and again He moves His little hands and feet,
smiling ever so slightly;
Shachi and Jagannatha, seeing this wonder,
gaze without blinking.

**87.** Every day she rubs His holy body
with fragrant oil and turmeric,
kisses His face and holds Him to her heart —
blessed is virtuous Shachi!

**88.** So day by day, moment by moment, bliss
grows in the town of Nadia;
they know not day or night, nor weekday nor lunar day;
in prema they forget themselves.

**89.** In the town of Nadia there is bliss in every house —
what to speak of men and women:
children, old men, the blind, bound by prema,
are drunk with matchless joy.

**90.** A face surpassing the autumn moon — they fancy
Madana himself dwells in it;
all the young women, as many as there were, went mad
and left their household duties to their elders.

**91.** Three times a day the women of the town run
to see the child;
"Let me see, let me see!" — all take Him on their laps,
and their bodies thrill with rapture.

**92.** So day by day, moment by moment —
can the bliss be told?
Hoping for the feet of Narahari Dasa,
Lochana Dasa sings [His] virtues.

*Thus ends the description of the Janma-lila.*

## Balya-lila (The Childhood Pastimes)

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* When six months had passed, Gaurasundara's first feeding with rice and naming were performed according to rule; since with His appearance the whole world was filled with bliss, the wise named Him Vishvambhara. Gradually Vishvambhara, holding His father's finger, began to walk about the courtyard. Before the lustre of Gaurasundara's body, adorned with armlets, bracelets, a pearl necklace and other ornaments, the radiance of millions of moons grows dim. [The moon] can dispel only outer darkness, not inner darkness, but the moon of Gaura dispels darkness both outer and inner. Shachi-devi used to lull her son to sleep singing "Come, come, O moon, come" and other songs; at times she saw various gods and goddesses come and praise her son, and was greatly amazed. At times, seeing Gaurahari dance wildly with the gods, crying "Radha-Govinda", she fell senseless. At times she heard the sound of ankle-bells on His bare feet and was astonished; at times, fearing some coming misfortune, she became very anxious; and the next moment, seeing her son's face, she forgot everything and kissed His face again and again. When some time had passed in this way, Gaurasundara began to play outside the house with His playmates. When Shachi-devi tried to catch Gaurasundara, He would run away; and at times, growing angry, He would come home and spoil everything in the house. At times He explained to His mother the worthlessness of worldly notions of pure and impure and instructed her in the transcendental knowledge that Krishna is the Lord of all. Then are described: how He sat in a pit full of pots of leftover food and instructed His mother; how He struck His mother and, seeing her fall senseless, brought a coconut; various childish pranks; His play with a puppy; how Gaurahari, angry with His mother for letting the puppy go, wept; how the puppy, in a divine body, singing of Hari, went to Vaikuntha; how Brahma and others, seeing the puppy's good fortune, praised Gaura; how Gaurahari wept when Shachi-devi prepared an offering for the worship of Shashthi, and under the guise of words revealed to Shachi that He is the Lord of all.

*Raga sindhura*

**93.** So day by day the body of Shachi's son
grows — like a stream of nectar.

**94.** What comparison shall I give Him? I can find none;
my heart trembles — I cannot tell it.

**95.** His moon-like face is a moon full in all sixteen digits every day;
even those blind from birth run, longing to see Him.

**96.** In rapture a half-smile plays on His lips,
like an ocean of nectar with rolling waves.

**97.** His two red eyes are drowning in rasa —
who has built a dam of the nectar-mud of kajal?

**98.** Virtuous Shachi and fortunate Jagannatha
both gaze lovingly at their son's face.

**99.** Now He laughs, now He cries, now He frolics,
now He is on her lap, now He swings on her breast.

**100.** Planting both His feet on Shachi's breast,
He sways like a golden creeper in the breeze.

**101.** Very long eyes, beautiful ringing laughter —
nectar seems to spill from His lips.

**102.** His nose more charming than a parrot's beak,
both cheeks radiant, His form beautiful.

[^09-1]: A has "upon living beings"; the 1983 edition "upon me".
[^09-2]: That is, not gross-bodied. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas stress that the Lord's birth is not a worldly conception: the eternal Lord, full of knowledge and bliss, first enters the heart of the father, then of the mother, and reveals Himself, as Krishna does in the *Bhagavata* (10.2.16–18); cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 13.83–88).
[^09-3]: An elder brahmana bowing to a young woman and circumambulating her goes against custom; but Advaita is worshipping the Lord in her womb.
[^09-4]: A play on words in the praise: "Advaita" ("non-dual") and "Nityananda" ("eternal bliss") are both epithets of the Lord and names of His closest associates.
[^09-5]: "Gadadhara" ("holder of the mace") is a name of Vishnu; perhaps there is also an allusion to Gadadhara Pandita, Gaura's inseparable companion.
[^09-6]: Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 13.89–98): the Lord appeared on the full moon of Phalguna during a lunar eclipse, when everyone, according to custom, was chanting the names of Hari.
[^09-7]: So after Bhaktisiddhanta's summary ("her house became Vaikuntha"); in A the line is corrupt; the 1983 edition has "in Shachi's womb the riches of the great Vaikuntha".
[^09-8]: Marks on the feet and body of a *mahapurusha*. The rendering of the last mark ("the elephant's kumbha", i.e. the frontal globe on an elephant's head) is tentative.
[^09-9]: Here and further on (vv. 59, 75, 90) Lochana Dasa describes the attraction of the women of Nadia to Gaura in the spirit of his *nagari-bhava*, in which Gaura is the *nagara*, the beloved of the townswomen. The Gaudiya tradition, following Vrindavana Dasa and Krishnadasa Kaviraja, does not accept worship of Gaura in such a bhava; Bhaktisiddhanta counts the "Gaura-nagari" among the deviant schools. We translate the text as it stands.
[^09-10]: In A this stanza is given in brackets together with the disha and is unnumbered; in the 1983 edition it is the first stanza of the song.
[^09-11]: Sindura is red powder, the mark of a married woman; pithali is rice paste for auspicious designs; they are distributed to women at a birth festival.
[^09-12]: The name comes from *nim*, the bitter neem tree: by custom a child is given a "bitter" name to avert misfortune and the evil eye. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 13.118), the name Nimai was given by Sita Thakurani to protect Him from witches.
[^09-13]: *Ashtakalai* — the distribution to children of eight kinds of pulses and grains on the eighth day after birth; on the ninth night, a festival with music.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 2 (portion 10)

*Balya-lila (continued): the first rice-feeding and the name Vishvambhara; the first steps; Shachi's lullaby; at night the gods enthrone the child, and He dances with them singing of Radha and Krishna; the sound of ankle-bells on bare feet; pranks and broken pots; a rite for the boy's welfare; the Lord sits among discarded pots and teaches His mother about pure and impure*

**103.** One, two, three, four, five, six months [passed] —
and on the day of the first rice-feeding the naming was performed.

**104.** Mishra Purandara celebrates a festival for his son;
the golden body [of the child] is decked with ornaments:

**105.** armlets and bracelets on His arms, a pearl necklace on His neck,
a golden chain around His waist, and anklets on His feet.

**106.** His palms and soles look as if rubbed with vermilion,
His lips are a bandhuli flower, His eyes red lotuses.

**107.** It is as if lightning had been rubbed onto Gora's limbs here and there:
His body glitters so that one cannot look at it.

**108.** Because He sustains the world, He was given the name Vishvambhara[^10-1]:
such was the message of Sarasvati — this is the Supreme Person.

**109.** Now, holding the fingers of father and mother,
He takes two steps and falls, unsteady on His feet.

**110.** Indistinctly, by halves, softly, softly He babbles —
as if nectar were welling up in an ocean of moonlight.

**111.** So day by day He toddles about the courtyard;
sufferings of every kind are gone, the world is cooled.

**112.** The feet that Lakshmi caresses are on the lap of the earth:
from love the goddess Prithivi forgets herself.

**113.** In the sky there is one moon, but on the ground ten moons[^10-2]:
the brilliance of their rays blinds the eyes of those who look.

**114.** And ten more moons are at the tips of His fingers:
if a sinner but looks, the darkness flees from his heart.

**115.** The moon of His beautiful face is king of many millions of moons;
His brows are Kama's bow: Kama worshipped [Him] by offering his bow.

**116.** What more can I say of the moonlight of His mercy?
It cuts through the darkness in the heart and does not spare it.[^10-3]

**117.** Who can tell of His childhood pastimes?
Behaving like an ordinary child, He sanctified the earth.

**118.** His elder brother is the worthy Vishvarupa:
endowed with every virtue, in a short time he mastered all the scriptures.

**119.** Who can tell the truth of his greatness —
of him whose younger brother is Mahaprabhu Vishvambhara?

**120.** Day by day the Lord reveals His mercy;
hearing of it, the heart of this Lochana Dasa rejoices.

*Raga varadi*

*Moon, moon, moon up in the sky —
who will pluck it and bring it to me?
Wiping off its stain,
I will paint a design on my Gora's forehead.
Come, come, come! My golden little son Nimai
is crying — He wants to sleep;
when He is fretful, every little word of His
is sweeter than nectar.*

**121.** "Nimai's father will come right now,
bringing thickened milk and bananas.
Look, little one, here comes the dreadful bogey[^10-4] —
close Your eyes and go to sleep!"

**122.** His face a golden lotus, His eyes red lotuses,
the pupils half closed —
it seems as if in a sea of honey
bees had half drowned.

**123.** Making a bed — a silk coverlet
with a fine-cloth quilt upon it —
Shachi Thakurani, taking her son in her arms,
lay down on her side.

**124.** One breast He holds in His mouth and sucks now and then,
and fingers the other with His little hand.
Lochana says: all this
is the play of the Crest-jewel of the gods in the body of a child.

*Raga dhanashi; disha: "Are are haya!" (murchha). "Listen to this wondrous tale, the song of Gora's virtues — a name auspicious to the ear! O-ki are, o-ki are haya!"*

**125.** Listen carefully to what happened on another day —
how the Lord of His own will revealed Himself.

**126.** In one room is Jagannatha, in another Shachi:
taking her son with her, Shachi sleeps happily.

**127.** The empty room filled with many soldiers and nobles;
seeing such a thing, Shachi was frightened.

**128.** All the gods came and, taking [Him] from Shachi's arms,
quickly seated [Him] on a jewelled throne.

**129.** Performing the bathing rite (abhisheka) and worship of many kinds,
they circle [Him] and fall down, clasping [His] feet.

**130.** All of them sound conches and bells again and again,
and cries of "Jaya, jaya!" and "Hari!" spread all around:

**131.** "Glory, glory to Jagannatha, the protector of all![^10-5]
In the Kali-yuga You will nourish us all.

**132.** You will give us all the rasa that is the treasure of Vrindavana.
This is our prayer at Your feet, O Vishvambhara!"

**133.** Seeing this, mother Shachi starts again and again;
"My son, my son!" Shachi cries in great fear.

**134.** She has no fear for herself — her life is in her son:
she sent the boy away to Jagannatha:

**135.** "Your father is sleeping there, in the Deity's room;
go there and sleep happily in his arms."

**136.** Vishvambhara went as His mother told Him —
and from His bare feet came the sound of ankle-bells.

**137.** When the Crest-jewel of the gods came out,
all the gods followed Him with joined palms.

**138.** The Lord said: "O gods, make Me dance:[^10-6]
sing of the pastimes of Radha and Krishna," — so He told them all.

**139.** The gods blended the prema of Radha and Krishna into their song
and offered it in bliss — and Gaurachandra took it up.

**140.** He Himself weeps and makes the gods weep;
"Radha, Radha, Govinda!" the Lord cries in the courtyard.

**141.** He calls out: "Kalindi, Yamuna, Vrindavana!" —
He calls "Radha, Radha!" in great bliss.

**142.** Seeing her son's play, Shachi fainted;
hearing the noise, Jagannatha came running in alarm.

**143.** Jagannatha calls: "Shachi, what sounds are these I hear?" —
and frightened Shachi-rani calls out loudly too.

**144.** Coming outside, both took their son in their arms;
seeing His bare feet, they forget themselves.

**145.** At once the deeds of Krishna came to their minds;
Shachi-devi told what she had seen in her room:

**146.** "The Four-faced one, the Five-faced one[^10-7] and all the other gods
came in celestial vehicles and served the boy.

**147.** In the courtyard our son danced, crying 'Radha-Krishna' —
I heard it too, but it seemed to me like a dream.

**148.** Seeing this, I sent Him to you in fear —
and I heard the sound of ankle-bells from His bare feet.

**149.** Such a boy, such a divine, beautiful form —
who knows when someone may cast an evil eye on Him?

**150.** Seven daughters of mine have died — this child is what is left;[^10-8]
if anything happens to Him, I shall not live any longer.

**151.** I have not many — [He is] the apple of my eye;
like a blind man's staff — such is my treasure.

**152.** Home, all I possess, wealth, the soul in the body, the body itself — [all this is He];
without Gorachand I cannot live.

**153.** Think of a remedy to ward off misfortune:
may all the gods, from first to last, grant the boy their blessing."

**154.** In such musings [the] night [passed], and in the morning
Shachi's son is playing with the boys.

**155.** Now He rolls about the courtyard, grey with dust;
seeing it, His mother speaks plaintively:

**156.** "Your body a golden doll, Your little face so lovely —
[even] the moon in the sky I cannot compare with it.

**157.** Why do You, with such a beautiful body, fall in the dust
and roll about in it? I implore You by Your mother's life!"

**158.** Saying this, she brushes off the dust and kisses His face;
her body thrills with rapture, her eyes grow red.

**159.** Then, some days later, Shachi's son
began to roam outside the house with His companions.

**160.** Under the trees on the bank of the Ganga He wanders, playing;
He plays the "monkey game" — standing on one leg.

**161.** Shachi heard that Gaurahari was on the bank of the Ganga
and went to catch Him with a switch in her hand.

**162.** Knee upon knee — He stands on one leg;
seeing it, His mother calls in a piercing voice.

**163.** Seeing His mother, the Lord runs away,
looking back like a frolicking elephant.

**164.** "Catch Him, catch Him!" shouts Shachi-rani;
ahead runs my Lord, the jewel of brahmanas.

**165.** Shachi wants to catch Him and cannot;
the Lord ran into the house.

**166.** All the pots and vessels that were in the house,
while [His mother cried] "catch Him, catch Him!", He smashed, dashing them to the ground.

**167.** Shachi stands with a finger to her nose and looks;
the Lord Vishvambhara stands with His face lowered.

**168.** He trembles all over with shame;
He stands with downcast face, tears streaming from His eyes —

**169.** as if wagtails sitting on the moon
were letting fall a pearl necklace they had swallowed.[^10-9]

**170.** Seeing Gora's face, Shachi was filled with love
and says: "Come into my arms, my darling!"

**171.** Taking His hand, lifting Him into her arms, Shachi-rani says:
"May my home and all I own be given up as Your ransom (nichhani)!"

**172.** Thus Gaurahari performs various pastimes;
Shachi cannot fathom her son's clever ways.

**173.** His boundless deeds are beyond the reach of worldly sense and of the Vedas;
not understanding His conduct, Shachi took it for naughtiness.

**174.** She decided that Nimai was an incorrigibly restless boy,
and in her sorrow Shachi-devi calls upon God.

**175.** One day, gathering all the elderly women,
she humbly asked them all:

**176.** "How I prayed — and God gave me a son;
but He behaves like a madman, He has no sense at all.

**177.** He does one thing and says another — I cannot understand;
He pays no heed to custom or to purity."

**178.** Hearing this, they all began to weep with sorrow;
taking Gorachand on their laps, they all say together:

**179.** "Why, why, child, do You do so much that is wrong?"
Hearing this, Vishvambhara became more restless than ever.

**180.** Seeing it, the women were pained at heart:
what Shachi had told, they saw [for themselves] at once.

**181.** "Since when has your son been like this?"
Shachi says: "I cannot even tell all of it.

**182.** One night I was sleeping with my son in my arms;
all the gods came and filled the room.

**183.** Seating my Nimai on a divine throne,
they fell at His feet and prostrated themselves.

**184.** Waking, I saw such a wonder;
since then — I do not know what spell is upon Him."

**185.** Hearing this, they all gave this opinion:
"It seems some god is dwelling in Him.

**186.** Begin a sacrifice in the name of all the gods:
tell Mishra to bring all the brahmanas.

**187.** Perform a svastyayana[^10-10] for the boy's welfare,
so that the god, having received worship, may go back to his abode.

**188.** Do not worry, Shachi," they said with certainty,
"having received worship, the god will free you from fear."

**189.** Taking the dust of their feet, [Shachi] bade them all farewell
and, going to Mishra, told him everything.

**190.** Hearing this, the anxious Mishra prepared all that was needed
and, summoning the brahmanas, performed the sacrifice.

**191.** Meanwhile Shachi took Gaurachandra to bathe in the Ganga,
thinking: "Now my son's restlessness is over."

**192.** Ahead of Shachi goes Vishvambhara Raya;
playing, He goes into an impure place.

**193.** Touching discarded pots, He walks on;
seeing it, His mother cries: "Alas, alas!

**194.** My son has become more restless still:
so this is what the svastyayana has done!"

**195.** "Shame, shame!" she cries and scolds Him harshly;
having heard her, Vishvambhara replies with kindly words:

**196.** "What is pure and impure, what is the truth of dharma and adharma?
Not discerning this, the world wanders astray.

**197.** Earth, water, air, fire, ether — these are [all] the forms;
in the whole world there is nothing but these.

**198.** There is no other dharma than the feet of Sri Krishna:
Krishna is the Lord of lords" — such was the essence He told.[^10-11]

**199.** Hearing this, Shachi-devi was amazed
and, taking Gauranga, bathed in the heavenly river.

**200.** Returning home, Shachi-devi says to Jagannatha:
"Listen, my lord, to what the boy has been doing.

**201.** This son of yours is the embodiment of all sacrifices:[^10-12]
I know for certain — this is no evil affliction.

**202.** Going into an impure place, He spoke such words —
never was it seen or heard that a child spoke so."

**203.** Hearing this, Jagannatha took his son in his arms:
though He had touched what is impure — all became auspicious.

**204.** "Lamp of our family, apple of my eye!
This body has no other soul than You."

**205.** Saying this, both gaze at their son's face;
overflowing with love, they forget themselves.

**206.** From their reddened eyes tears flow in a hundred streams,
their whole bodies thrill, their words come in halves.

**206+1.** Then Vishvambhara, together with Vishvarupa,[^10-13]
plays various games, with these songs and dances.

**206+2.** They are like Indra and Upendra, two brothers born of one mother;[^10-14]
seeing them, Shachi and Jagannatha rejoice at heart.

**207.** Looking into each other's faces, both burst out laughing;
happily this Lochana Dasa sings of Gora's virtues.

[^10-1]: Vishvambhara — "He who sustains (supports) the universe". In Bhaktisiddhanta's summary the name is given by the wise, because with the Lord's appearance the whole world was filled with bliss; in Murari Gupta (1.06.3) the father gives the name.
[^10-2]: The ten moons on the ground are His toenails; in the next couplet, His fingernails.
[^10-3]: Cf. Bhaktisiddhanta's summary: the moon dispels only outer darkness, but the moon of Gaura dispels both outer and inner darkness.
[^10-4]: *Hau* — a bogey with which children are frightened in Bengal.
[^10-5]: "Jagannatha" — "Lord of the universe"; it is both an epithet of the Lord and the name of His father.
[^10-6]: So in the 1983 and 1903 editions (*nachaha amare*); the OCR of Bhaktisiddhanta's edition can also be read *na chaha amare* — "do not look at Me (do not worship Me)". The following lines (the gods sing, the Lord dances) support the first reading.
[^10-7]: The Four-faced one is Brahma, the Five-faced one Shiva.
[^10-8]: According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 13.73), before Vishvarupa eight daughters were born to Shachi one after another and died; Lochana Dasa speaks of seven.
[^10-9]: The eyes are wagtails (*khanjana*), the face the moon, the tears pearls.
[^10-10]: *Svastyayana* — a rite to avert misfortune and invoke well-being.
[^10-11]: Cf. Bhaktisiddhanta's summary: the Lord explained to His mother the worthlessness of worldly notions of pure and impure and instructed her in the transcendental knowledge that Krishna is the Lord of all. The point of v. 197 is not that only matter exists: distinctions of pure and impure concern combinations of the same material elements, while true dharma is service to Krishna's feet. Cf. Murari Gupta (1.06.13–17).
[^10-12]: Shachi guesses that her son is the Yajna-purusha Himself, the Lord of all sacrifices, so that a rite to appease a "possessing god" is out of place here.
[^10-13]: Vv. 206+1 – 206+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them in brackets — the way that editor marks lines found in only some manuscripts.
[^10-14]: Upendra is Lord Vamana, the younger brother of Indra (both are sons of Aditi); so here Vishvarupa is the elder brother and Vishvambhara the younger.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 3 (portion 11)

*Balya-lila (continued): Nimai pretends not to hear His mother; He sits again on the discarded pots, throws a brick at His mother and brings two coconuts; Shachi for a moment realizes that her son is Narayana, but forgets it again in love for her son and recites protective names over Him; Nimai cries for the moon from the sky; His play with a puppy*

*Raga shri; disha: "O-ki ho re, Gauranga, jaya, jaya!" (murchha). "O-ki na, my prema for Gauranga — nectar and bliss! Ki na, my Gauranga, ki are, jaya, jaya!"*

**208.** So day by day, changing every moment,
His body grows, shapely as Mount Sumeru.

**209.** The sweetness of His speech is like a stream of nectar;
hearing it, Shachi-devi rejoices at heart.

**210.** On the pretext of conversation the queen wants to hear Him speak;
the Lord says: "I can't hear what you say."

**211.** Shachi calls Him loudly, in great merriment;
"I can't hear!" says Gora, who wears the forest garland.

**212.** Mother Shachi, enchanted by parental love (vatsalya),
angrily seizes a switch and runs like a madwoman:

**213.** "Today You won't listen to [my] words, like a naughty boy —
in my old age You won't even give me rice to eat!"

**214.** And some days later the son of Shachi,
being stubborn, does not listen to His mother's words.

**215.** Shachi-devi grew angry, stared at Him fixedly,
and ran to catch Him with a switch in her hand.

**216.** Vishvambhara ran off to the impure place
where useless earthen pots are thrown away.

**217.** Seeing it, His mother strikes her head with her hand;
Shachi laments and scolds Him with harsh words.

**218.** Vishvambhara grew angrier still at heart:
He climbed onto the pots heaped one upon another and stood on them.

**219.** Hearing angry words, He does the opposite;
understanding this, His mother spoke gently:

**220.** "Come, come here, child, leave this disgusting business!
It is not fitting for You — it is not the dharma of a brahmana.

**221.** You are a brahmana's son, and of a noble (kulina) family besides;
what will people say when they hear of such shameful conduct?

**222.** Come, come, child, bathe in the water of the Ganga;
Your mother's heart is breaking — come, climb into my arms.

**223.** Otherwise I will throw myself into the Ganga and die —
and You will go weeping from house to house.

**224.** Your body is gold of the finest purity, tested on the touchstone:
why do You smear soot on such a beautiful body?

**225.** Leave this impure, foul place, my child:
the soot on Your body is like the stain on the moon."

**226.** Hearing this, Vishvambhara, the treasury of virtues, grew angry:
"Again and again I tell you — and still you do not understand!

**227.** Saying 'impure, impure', you speak ill words;
what is pure and what impure — judge that first concerning Me!"

**228.** Saying this, He took in His hand a brick lying before Him
and struck His mother on the head with the brick.

**229.** At the blow Shachi-rani fell into a seeming swoon;[^11-1]
"Mother, mother!" He Himself weeps.

**230.** Hearing the loud weeping, the women of the town
who were nearby came running at once.

**231.** Sprinkling Ganga water on her face, they brought her round;
as soon as she came to, she called: "Vishvambhara!"

**232.** Opening her arms, Shachi took her son on her lap;
coming out of her swoon, she forgot what had happened before.

**233.** Vishvambhara weeps, looking at His mother;
then a lovely woman said with a laugh,

**234.** taking Vishvambhara by the chin:
"Go and bring your mother two coconuts —

**235.** then your mother Shachi will live;
otherwise she will die — listen to me."

**236.** Hearing this, Vishvambhara was glad
and at once brought a pair of coconuts —

**237.** their stalks just broken off, fresh and full of juice;
He placed the two coconuts before His mother.

**238.** Seeing this, the women were amazed:
"Where did a child get coconuts in a moment?"

**239.** There was a lovely, playful woman there;
very softly she asks Vishvambhara:

**240.** "You are a child — where did You get the coconuts?
I have understood something about Your doings!"

**241.** Hearing such words, Vishvambhara Raya
gave a fierce "hum!" and clasped His mother's neck.

**242.** Coming to herself, Shachi took her son on her lap
and covered His lotus face with countless kisses.

**243.** With the end of her cloth she wiped His face and body
and washed His holy body with the water of the heavenly river.

**244.** Having bathed Him as if with an abhisheka of Ganga water,
she gazes in wonder at her son's face:

**245.** deep as the ocean, with the splendour of millions of suns;
His twenty nails shine like millions of moons;

**246.** beauty surpassing millions of Kamas, a graceful body,
playful, curving eyes, brows like Kama's bow.

**247.** The Lord of all the worlds has appeared on earth —
seeing it, the mother felt awe in her heart.

**248.** The secret of the past came back: when she was carrying Him in her womb,
she had seen the gods in celestial vehicles.

**249.** And all that He had done before in His childhood play —
all of it now came back to her mind.

**250.** She knew for certain: this is the radiant, eternal,
untainted, pure Narayana, without [mundane] form,[^11-2]

**251.** all-pervading, bearer of all energies, satisfied in Himself,
the incomparable object of meditation of the great yogis.

**252.** "No one can measure my good fortune —
not Brahma, nor Maheshvara, nor the other gods:

**253.** this son of mine is the One whom all worship!"
Saying this, she took Gauraraya into her arms.

**254.** But as soon as Shachi took Gaurahari into her arms,
in her love for her son Shachi-devi forgot His majesty.[^11-3]

**255.** Shachi came home in wonder:
"What god has manifested through my son?"

**256.** Thinking this, she ties a protective thread and, touching His body,
says: "Janardana, Hrishikesha, Govinda!

**257.** May the Sudarshana disc guard Your head,
may Narayana protect Your eyes, nose and mouth;

**258.** may Lord Gadadhara guard Your chest,
may Lord Giridhara guard Your arms;

**259.** may Damodara guard Your belly and groin,
may Lord Nrisimha guard Your navel;

**260.** may Lord Trivikrama guard both Your knees,
may Dharadhara guard Your feet."[^11-4]

**261.** Mother Shachi blows upon all His limbs
and, in love for her son, is utterly beside herself.

**262.** So in joy and merriment the day passed;
the blessed hour came — evening fell.

**263.** Happily Shachi left Gaurahari in the courtyard
and sent the servants and maids to do the evening tasks.

**264.** So the day came to an end, evening fell,
and the full moon of the full-moon day rose in the sky.

**265.** Then Vishvambhara, clever and all-knowing,
began to call "Mama, mama!" as if He knew nothing.

**266.** Shachi says: "Don't cry at evening time;
whatever You want I will give — do as I say."

**267.** The Lord says: "Pluck the moon and give it to Me!"
Laughing, Shachi replies: "Oh, You silly child!

**268.** What a son I've been given in my house!
Who can catch the moon in the sky?"

**269.** The Lord says: "You said, 'Whatever You want I will give' —
why then did you say such words?

**270.** That is why I wanted to take the moon."
Saying this, He wept loudly.

**271.** Clutching the end of her sari, He cries and throws tantrums of every kind,
stamps His feet, rubs His eyes.

**272.** Clinging to His mother's neck, Gora Raya weeps:
He wants the moon from the sky to play with.

**273.** Now He sulks, now rolls on the ground, now tears His mother's hair;
all grey with dust, He beats His own head with His hand.

**274.** Seeing it, His mother says: "Silly child,
Your doings are a marvel to me!

**275.** Where can I catch the moon in the sky?
How many such moons there are on Your body!

**276.** Look: the moon has grown pale with shame —
without thinking, it rose in front of You.

**277.** Not knowing of the rising of the moon of Navadvipa,
it was ashamed and has gone to hide in the clouds.

**278.** Listen: the bogey[^11-5] has come to Navadvipa!
Don't cry, my child, my life!"

**279.** Saying this, she takes Him in her arms and kisses His face,
and, happy in the bliss of love, the goddess forgets herself.

**280.** In joy and merriment Shachi, overwhelmed by her good fortune,
watching her son's play, cannot tell one direction from another.

**281.** Shachi's heart rejoices, her speech falters;
happily this Lochana Dasa sings of Gora's virtues.

*Raga dhanashi.[^11-6] "Glory, glory, glory to Shachi's son, the youthful source of bliss! With the boys He plays in many ways, performing childish pastimes."*

**282.** Taking along all the lively children,
He plays various games;
with the boys, in childish sport,
He performs playful pastimes.

**283.** As they played, suddenly [they saw] there
two or four puppies;
the fun grew: choosing one,
Gaurahari took it.

**284.** The boys who were with Him said to Him:
"Listen, listen, Vishvambhara:
You've left us the ugly ones and taken the good one for Yourself —
we won't play, we're going home!"

**285.** Then Vishvambhara replied:
"This puppy belongs to everyone;
all of you play with it together,
but it will live at My house."

**286.** Saying this, He took the puppy
and went to His own house;
at home He put a rope round its neck
and tied it on the raised step of the house.

**287.** Meanwhile Vishvambhara's mother,
having finished her housework,
went to the bank of the Ganga to bathe
together with the women of the town.

**288.** Then Vishvambhara, finding the house empty,
took the puppy
and plays with the boys in every way,
grey with dust.

**289.** As they played with the boys,
two of them quarrelled;
then Gaurahari, taking one's side,
began to scold the other:

**290.** "Every day you come and start a quarrel —
what sort of nature is that?
I see what you are and how you behave:
you are a puppy thief!"

**291.** Then that boy, angry at heart,
ran outside
and shouted at the top of his voice before Shachi,
choking with rage:

**292.** "Listen, your Vishvambhara
has taken a puppy:
now He holds it in His arms, now hugs it round the neck —
come and see for yourself!"

**293.** Hearing the boy's words,
Shachi-rani hurried home
and sees with her own eyes:
Vishvambhara is holding the puppy in His arms.

**294.** Striking her head with her hand, the mother says:
"I can't make out Your games!
There are all kinds of games, and You — how perverse! —
are playing with a puppy.

**295.** Your father is a most pious man,
and You are his son:
what will people say? With a puppy
You play — what pleasure do You find in it?

**296.** You are a brahmana's son —
yet there is nothing of such conduct in You;
whoever hears of this — who will speak well of it?
It is a spear in my heart.

**297.** You are so beautiful —
what joy is there in smearing Yourself with dust?
I speak to You, and You lower Your eyes —
may my mouth be burned!

**298.** Your face surpasses many moons,
Your body is steady lightning;
yet You care nothing for fine clothes, You smear dust on Your body,
You keep company with bad boys."

**299.** In anger Shachi-devi, biting her lip,
scolds the boy:
"Go home, take the puppy,
make a present of it to your father and mother!"

**300.** Saying this, she looks at her son's face
and calls Him, beside herself with joy:
"Come, come, child, climb into my arms,
let me kiss Your face!

**301.** Let the puppy go, child,
and bathe in the Ganga's water;
it is already midday — aren't You hungry?
How much grief You cause me!

**302.** Or else tie up the puppy, child,
and go to bathe;
in the afternoon You can play and take the puppy,
but now eat something.

**303.** Your face, a golden lotus, has grown pale,
as if withered in the sun;
drops of sweat stand on the tip of Your nose —
it breaks my heart to see!"

**304.** Hearing His mother's answer, Vishvambhara
laughed, stood up and says:
"If My puppy goes anywhere,
then you yourself will find out!"

**305.** Saying this, Hari hugged His mother's neck
and goes to bathe;
she brushed off His dust, wiped His face
and rubbed His body with fragrant oil.

**306.** To bathe He goes to the bank of the Ganga,
taking His companions along,
and in the water of the heavenly river, in great merriment,
He frolics and splashes.

**307.** They all splash one another with water
like frolicking elephants;
and Gora's body is like Mount Sumeru —
unshakeable, wondrous.

**308.** Meanwhile Shachi-devi, after some thought,
let the puppy go;
seeing its own mother, it ran after her —
who knows where it went.

**309.** There was a boy there —
he ran to the bank of the Ganga:
"Listen, Vishvambhara: your mother
has let the puppy go!"

**310.** Hearing the boy's words,
He came running at once
and went to look in the place
where the puppy had been.

**311.** He looks in all four directions — no puppy;
His heart blazed with anger.
He weeps loudly and scolds His mother,
grieving for the puppy:

**312.** "Listen, foolish one, what have you done, mother?
What grief you have caused Me!
The most beautiful, the best of puppies —
how could you give it to someone?"

**313.** Shachi-rani says: "But I don't know
[where] your puppy is;
it was here — someone took it somewhere;
what a thieving boy!

**314.** Why do You cry
over a puppy?
Whoever took it, I'll take the trouble
to ask for it and bring it tomorrow.

**315.** Enough now! I swear to You
by my own oath:
I will bring the puppy to You —
don't cry, don't cry now!"

**316.** Saying this, she wiped His face
and took her son in her arms;
gazing at His beautiful face, joyfully
she covered Him with countless kisses.

**317.** Rubbing His body and making Him clean,
she bathed Him in the Ganga's water
and fed Him well with sweets — sandesh and modakas,
thickened milk and bananas.

**318.** On His head are three tufts, with five knots on them,
tied together;
in His eyes is kajal, a clear, fine line;
His glance delights the world.

**319.** A red-bordered dhoti is wrapped round His waist,
its end swinging down to His feet;
on His breast a pearl necklace,
on His forehead a sandal tilaka.

**320.** Armlets and bracelets, priceless jewels,
magara anklets on His feet;
He goes to the boys to play,
holding a milk laddu in His hand.

**321.** His gait is beautiful, surpassing an elephant's,
His speech deep and sweet;
Gora, king of the twice-born, among the boys —
the moon surrounded by stars.[^11-7]

**322.** In such pastimes the Lord plays —
the gods watch and are swept away in bliss;
cats and dogs the Lord touches —
Lochana Dasa marvels.

[^11-1]: So in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition (*kapata murchchha* — "a feigned, seeming swoon"); the 1983 and 1903 editions have simply "she fainted". In Murari Gupta (1.06.22) Shachi loses consciousness from the blow. The word "seeming" indicates that this is no worldly misfortune but play: the Lord's mother is His eternal associate, and her swoon is part of the lila.
[^11-2]: The words "untainted, pure, without form" (*nirlepa, niranjana, nirakara*) are taken from the language of the Upanishads. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 6, the conversation with Sarvabhauma), understand such epithets as denying only material qualities and a material form: the Lord has an eternal form full of being, knowledge and bliss — the very form Shachi sees before her (vv. 245–246).
[^11-3]: Cf. the *Bhagavata* (10.8.43): having seen the universe in Krishna's mouth, Yashoda by the Lord's will again forgot His majesty and saw in Him only her son. This is the working of yogamaya, which nourishes vatsalya-rasa, not worldly ignorance.
