[^11-4]: A protective prayer assigning names of Vishnu to each limb — as the gopis protected the infant Krishna after the Putana episode (*Bhagavata* 10.6.21–29). Giridhara — "the lifter of the hill" (Krishna); Trivikrama — Vamana; Dharadhara — "the upholder of the earth" (Varaha or Shesha).
[^11-5]: *Hau* — a bogey used to frighten children (cf. en/10, v. 121).
[^11-6]: In A an unclear word follows (perhaps an indication of metre).
[^11-7]: "King of the twice-born" (*dvijaraja*) — both the best of brahmanas and the moon.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 4 (portion 12)

*Balya-lila (continued): by Gaura's touch the puppy gains divine knowledge and departs for Goloka in a celestial chariot, while the gods praise Gaura's mercy; Nimai eats the offering Shachi has prepared for the goddess Shashthi and teaches her that worship of Achyuta is the worship of all the gods; Nimai mimics Murari Gupta expounding yoga and instructs him in bhakti*

**324.** By the touch of Gauranga the puppy became fortunate:
giving up its nature, it gained divine knowledge.[^12-1]

**325.** It cries "Radha-Krishna, Govinda!" and dances;
seeing it, all the people of Nadia run after it.

**326.** Seeing such rapture in the puppy,
all thrill with ecstasy, their eyes full of tears.

**327.** Suddenly the fortunate one left its dog's body
and, becoming a dweller of Krishna's realm, set out for Goloka.

**328.** All at once a divine chariot appeared
and carries him away along the path of the sky.

**329.** A golden chariot, beautiful, with a thousand pinnacles,
glitters with pendants of jewels and pearls.

**330.** Hundreds of thousands of bells ring on it,
bronze cymbals sound in crowds.

**340.** The sound of conches is heard, cries of "Jaya!" and "Hari!";[^12-2]
gandharvas and kinnaras sing of Radha and Krishna.

**341.** Banners and pennants all fly above the chariot;
their shining radiance eclipses the disc of the sun.

**342.** In the middle of the chariot, on a jewelled throne,
sits he, lovely, of enchanting lustre.

**343.** Divine ornaments adorn every limb;
millions of Madanas swoon with shame.

**344.** Cool, surpassing millions of moons,
he cries out: "Radha-Krishna! Gauranga!"

**345.** The siddhas came, fanning [him] with chamaras,
and bore him along the path to Goloka.

**346.** Brahma, Shiva, Sanaka and the others, all with joined palms,
surrounding the chariot, sing the glory of Gauranga:

**347.** "Glory, glory to the ocean of mercy, the son of Shachi!
Never has the Lord shown such mercy!

**348.** He delivered a dog and sent it to Goloka —
no one has ever attained such a divine body!

**349.** Glory, glory to Gaurahari, the refuge of the helpless!
Glory, glory to Him who is above all avataras!

**350.** By Your mercy the souls of Kali will be delivered;
what superhuman pastimes will You not show!

**351.** When shall we gods become fortunate
and gain the supreme gift — the grace of Your feet?

**352.** By Your touch even a dog is delivered —
such mercy was never found even in Hrishikesha![^12-3]

**353.** When shall we too have such good fortune?
You blessed a dog — that same we beg for ourselves.

**354.** Obeisance, obeisance to Gauraraya, who sees no faults!
Obeisance, obeisance to Your two beautiful feet!"

**355.** So, following [the chariot], all the gods [pray]:
"When shall we attain the feet of Gaurachandra?"

**356.** And that greatly fortunate one arrived in Goloka
and there ceaselessly sings of Gauranga's pastimes.

**357.** Such is the wondrous manifestation of Gorachand —
in bliss this Lochana Dasa sings [His] virtues.

**358.** Now Shachi-devi resolved
to keep the vow of Shashthi:[^12-4]
all the women of the town, keeping the vow,
[gathered] under the banyan tree.

**359.** Having properly prepared the offering (naivedya)
and covering it with the end of her sari,
she goes to the banyan to keep the vow,
in great joy.

**360.** At that very moment Vishvambhara Raya,
playing on the road,
saw His mother and came running:
"What are you carrying in your hands?"

**361.** Spreading His arms, blocking the way,
He tries to stop His mother;
"What is it, what?" He keeps saying and tries to grab it,
fretting before His mother.

**362.** "I have carefully prepared the offering
for the worship of the goddess,
and I am going to the banyan to worship Shashthi —
You play here.

**363.** On my way back, listen, child,
I will bring You prasada;
I will worship the goddess and ask her for this boon —
that the fever of misfortune may go."

**364.** Hearing such a reply and knowing His mother's heart,
Sri Vishvambhara
speaks gentle words —
a nectarean loveliness spreads over His lips:

**365.** "How many times have I told you —
you don't understand, foolish one!
With hunger
I am burning inside: I will eat the offering."

**366.** Saying this, Gaurahari seized
the offering and filled His mouth with it;
seeing it, His mother cried out in dismay —
her heart blazed with grief.

**367.** Seeing that Vishvambhara had eaten what was meant for the goddess —
ghee, honey, milk-foods —
Shachi, her heart pounding,
her eyes brimming with angry tears, [says]:

**368.** "Silly child, how much must I teach You!
You do not honour the gods.
A brahmana's son — and so ill-behaved!
I shall die of this grief."

**369.** Hearing His mother's words,
the jewel Gaura blazed with anger:
"I told you all this — and still you do not understand
and speak ill words to Me.

**370.** Listen, foolish one: I know everything,
I am the essence of the three worlds;
whatever there is in the world is I alone,
in the three worlds there is no other.[^12-5]

**371.** As watering the root of a tree
nourishes the branches above,
as nourishing the life-breath (prana) [satisfies] the senses —
such is the law concerning Me."

*So in the Srimad-Bhagavata (4.31.14):*

**371a.** "As by watering the root of a tree its trunk, branches and twigs are satisfied,
and as by food offered to the prana the senses [are satisfied] —
so worship of Achyuta is the worship of all."[^12-6]

**372.** Saying this, Hari playfully
put His arms round His mother's neck;
and Shachi, astonished at heart,
went to worship Shashthi.

**373.** Having served the goddess Shashthi in many ways,
she speaks plaintively:
"My child is a great rascal;
please forgive His fault.

**374.** You yourself gave me this wild boy —
how can you take offence at Him?
Grant your blessing to this son of mine,
do not be angry at all.

**375.** I have not many — this treasure Nimai
you gave me out of mercy;
let no misfortune befall my son —
this boy is yours, O goddess!"

**376.** Saying this, she falls at the feet
of all the elderly women,
humbly entreats them and bows:
"Bless Him from your hearts!

**377.** Calling [Him] your own, place the dust of your feet
on the head of my Vishvambhara;
this child of mine is very restless —
may His mind become steady."

**378.** Holding a blade of grass between her teeth,[^12-7] Shachi-rani says,
serving the feet of all:
"Bless Him, all of you — may my son Vishvambhara
live long!"

**379.** Having performed the worship of Shashthi and taking her son by the hand,
the goddess went home;
with Jagannatha she reflects,
pondering what she had experienced.

**380.** What more shall I say? The essence of all the gods
has appeared on earth
and plays with the boys in many ways —
says Lochana Dasa.

*Raga varadi*[^12-8]

**381.** And some days later that son of Shachi
plays in the dust on the highway;
His body a golden mountain, grey with dust,
with Him His devoted companions.

**382.** The children play with dust, now and then they squabble,
in the dust-battle their bodies are bare;
all of one age, all of one mind,
drops of sweat from the effort of play.

**383.** While they were all playing together, Gupta the vaidya[^12-9]
suddenly came along that road;
all his own people went with him,
and he, a scholar, was discoursing on the path of knowledge.

**384.** Reasoning with them, he expounds the yoga-shastra,
shaking his head and waving his hands;
seeing this, Vishvambhara Raya runs right behind him,
imitating his gait and speech.

**385.** Murari the vaidya, glancing [at Him] for a moment,
again expounds yoga;
just so Vishvambhara expounds yoga,
just so He moves His hands and head.

**386.** So again and again Gaurahari
mimics him together with the children;
Murari the vaidya, seeing his own manners mimicked,
grew angry and spoke rude words:

**387.** "Who would call this one good? I've seen the boy —
this is Mishra Purandara's son;
everywhere I hear talk and songs of his virtues —
a fine name he has: Nimai!"[^12-10]

**388.** Hearing such words, the jewel Gaura grew angry —
for the sake of mercy to His devotee;
frowning, He says teasingly:
"You'll find out at mealtime!"[^12-11]

**389.** Hearing Vishvambhara's words, Murari pondered
and went home with a wondering heart;
busy with household affairs, his mind turned elsewhere and he forgot,
and then it was time to eat.

**390.** Meanwhile Vishvambhara Hari dressed Himself finely:
He wrapped His dhoti tightly round His waist,
three tufts adorn His head, rasakanti beads hang on His neck,
a double string of pearls at His throat.

**391.** A line of kajal in His eyes, His topknot tied with five knots,
golden ornaments glittering,
magara anklets on His feet; holding a milk laddu in His hand,
the Lord Vishvambhara set out.

**392.** He entered Murari Gupta's house, into the inner rooms,
where the best of vaidyas was eating his meal,
and in a voice deep as thunder, by His own grace,
He called: "Murari!"

**393.** Hearing the voice, Gupta the vaidya remembered
what Vishvambhara had said, and was startled;
and Gaurahari, saying "What are you doing, what are you doing?",
came right up to the spot.

**394.** "Don't be alarmed: I am here —
go on eating," He said;
and in the middle of the meal, approaching very slowly,
He filled his plate with urine.

**395.** "What's this, what?! Shame, shame!" — up jumped Murari;
and Gora, clapping His hands, says:
"Shaking his head and waving his hands, having left the path of bhakti,
this trickster expounds yoga!

**396.** Disregarding knowledge and ritual, worship Krishna wholeheartedly —
the relisher of rasa, the expert, full of consciousness and bliss.
One whose sight is fixed on the material finds no nourishment for bhajana;
you do not understand — your intelligence is very dull.

**397.** Hari is supremely merciful, He holds all energies;
is such a thing possible for a jiva?[^12-12]
He is the eternal Brahman, the treasure of the gopis' life;
why, not worshipping Him, do you cause [yourself] pain?"

**398.** Saying this, the jewel Gaura vanished — none knows where;
Murari cannot see Him
and reflects within himself: "This is surely no one else —
truly, the son of Shachi is the Lord."

**399.** Having reflected thus, Murari
set out at once, in haste and agitation;
from great joy he can hardly walk on the road,
[yet] he came to where Mishra Purandara was.

[^12-1]: In the 1903 edition the whole account of the puppy's deliverance (vv. 324–357) is enclosed in brackets — this editor's way of marking lines found not in all manuscripts. In Bhaktisiddhanta's summary the puppy "in a divine body, singing of Hari, went to Vaikuntha"; the text says Goloka ("becoming a dweller of Krishna's realm"; the 1983 edition: "of Vishnu's realm"). Cf. the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Antya 1.16–33): Shivananda Sena's dog, having received the remnants of Mahaprabhu's meal and chanting "Krishna", attained Vaikuntha.
[^12-2]: In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition the numbering jumps here from 330 to 340; we keep the edition's numbers.
[^12-3]: That is, even Krishna in His earlier pastimes did not bestow mercy so freely. Cf. the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 19.53): Gaura is "the most magnanimous", the giver of prema for Krishna.
[^12-4]: Shashthi — the goddess who protects children; women honour her with fasting and offerings under a banyan tree for the welfare of their children.
[^12-5]: This is not an assertion of impersonal oneness: all that exists is the energy of the Lord and does not exist apart from Him, while He remains the personal Lord. Cf. the *Bhagavata* (2.9.33): "Before creation I alone existed…", and Mahaprabhu's teaching of inconceivable oneness and difference (*achintya-bhedabheda*). This is the sense the following shloka brings out.
[^12-6]: In the edition the shloka is followed by Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya and translation (the OCR is almost illegible); the translation runs: "As watering the root of a tree satisfies its trunk, branches, twigs and so on, and as food offered to the prana satisfies all the senses, so by worshipping Achyuta alone all the gods are worshipped." In the 1903 edition the shloka is given in square brackets as an editorial insertion.
[^12-7]: A blade of grass between the teeth is a sign of utmost humility before those whose favour is asked.
[^12-8]: In A the raga line is corrupt; "varadi" follows the 1983 edition.
[^12-9]: Murari Gupta, a vaidya (physician) by caste, later one of Mahaprabhu's closest associates and author of the Sanskrit biography *Sri Krishna-chaitanya-charitamrita*. Here he is still devoted to yoga and the path of knowledge.
[^12-10]: A gibe: "Nimai" comes from the bitter neem tree (see en/09, note 12).
[^12-11]: Lochana Dasa stresses that the Lord's "anger" is mercy: He breaks Murari's attachment to dry yoga and knowledge so as to turn him to bhakti.
[^12-12]: That is, the perfections of which yoga speaks are attributes of the Lord; the jiva cannot acquire them by its own efforts — its path is service to Him.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 5 (portion 13)

*Balya-lila (conclusion): Murari Gupta prostrates himself before the child Nimai, reveals to His parents that their son is Bhagavan, and tells Advaita Acharya about Him; Nimai and the boys hold a kirtana, and passing pandits join them; Murari tells Damodara Pandita of Vishvarupa's sannyasa; the grief of Shachi and Jagannatha; Vishvambhara promises to take care of His parents*

**400.** Here, meanwhile, Shachi and Jagannatha together
are fondling their son: "You are my whole wealth!
Wherever I go, whatever sorrow I meet,
I look at Your moonlike face, and all is forgotten."

**401.** Saying this, both kiss Him on both cheeks
and vie with each other to take Him on their lap.
At that very moment Murari came straight there —
from joy he cannot utter a word.

**401a.** Seeing him, Shachi and Jagannatha hastily
rose and went to greet the vaidya;
but he said nothing to anyone and forgot everything else,
gazing at Gorachand's face.[^13-1]

**393a.** His whole body thrills with ecstasy
from head to foot; streams of tears flow from his eyes;
his reddened eyes — these are the witnesses of prema;
in a faltering voice he babbles half-words.

**394a.** Unable to stand steady, he falls at His feet
and bows down again and again;
seeing this, Vishvambhara crept
into His mother's lap, as if He understood nothing.

**395a.** Shachi and Jagannatha say: "Ah, what have you done, what have you done!
We regard you as equal to a god.
This very little boy is fit for your blessing —
what a great breach of custom you have committed![^13-2]

**396a.** All people extol you, calling you a sage among the shudras;[^13-3]
what offence has my boy committed against you?
Whatever may come, let it fall on me — may the child's years increase!
Bless Him, saying, 'Live long!'"

**397a.** Saying this, holding his hands, bowing and entreating,
[stand] Shachi and Mishra Purandara;
and Murari, smiling, says: "This son of yours,
Vishvambhara, is the God of the god of gods.

**398a.** Cherish the boy, keep Him close to you — later you will understand this;
no one is as fortunate as you.
Keep these words of mine treasured in your heart, guarding the secret:
Vishvambhara is that very Bhagavan."

**399a.** Saying this, Gupta the vaidya spoke of nothing else
and went away with a joyful heart;
his heart overflowing with bliss, having seen Gora's feet,
he went to the house of the Acharya.

**400a.** His name is Advaita Acharya: He is the abode of all virtues,
He is the teacher of all people.
Falling at His feet, Murari humbly says:
"You are the wish-fulfilling tree for all devotees.

**401b.** I have seen a wonder: the son of Mishra Purandara,
Nimai Pandita Vishvambhara,
merrily plays His childhood games with all the boys —
I have seen deeds beyond the human."

**402.** Hearing this, the jewel of the twice-born thundered "hum-hum";
His whole body filled with thrills of ecstasy:
"This is a secret, a secret — I tell you in private:
He is that very Brahman, the Relisher of rasa, in a beautiful form."[^13-4]

**403.** Saying this, they embraced, both forgetting themselves in joy,
but they do not disclose their conviction [to others].
The Lord of all the worlds has come to earth out of mercy —
His glories are sung by this Lochana Dasa.

*Raga bhatiyari; disha (numbered as a couplet in the edition):*

**404.** The cry "Hari! Hari!" is heard, filling all the directions;
clapping His hands, [crying] "Jaya, jaya!", the jewel of the twice-born dances.

**405.** Gathering together all the boys of His age,
He has started a fine game — a kirtana of Hari's glories.

**406.** Surrounding Him on all sides, the boys cry "Hari, Hari!"
and the Lord, overwhelmed with joy, rolls on the ground.

**407.** "Sing, sing!" He calls in a voice deep as thunder,
and saying, "Come here, come!", He embraces the boys.

**408.** At the touch of His divine body a boy forgets himself —
see how the boy, overwhelmed, is weeping!

**409.** From head to foot — thrills of ecstasy; streams of tears flow;
clapping his hands, the boy cries "Hari, Hari!"

**410.** The boys surround Him on all sides, and in the middle is the lion Gora,
as if intoxicated bees had encircled a honey-filled lotus.

**411.** At that time a few pandits were passing along that road
and suddenly saw Vishvambhara's game.

**412.** They see the wonderful game of the boy Gora:
on everyone's neck are garlands strung of wildflowers;

**413.** with their mouths they cry "Hari, Hari!" and clap their hands,
and in the middle Gorahari dances about in joy.

**414.** Forgetting themselves, all the pandits joined them,
and, clapping their hands, they too cried "Hari, Hari!"

**415.** Whoever comes or goes along the road is entranced at the sight;
women with water-pots on their hips stand and gaze.

**416.** Hearing the cries of "Hari, Hari!" and the shouts of "Jaya, jaya!",
some came running, eager to see.

**417.** Hearing these cries, Shachi suddenly came
and saw her own son Nimai and the pandits.

**418.** Crying "My son, my son!", Shachi took Nimai in her arms,
and, looking at them all, she speaks harsh words:

**419.** "Fine conduct for a whole assembly of pandits!
You have driven someone else's son mad and make him dance like a lunatic!"

**420.** At her harsh words they all came to their senses:
"What have we done, what have we done?" each thinks to himself.

**421.** Vishvambhara's mother took Vishvambhara away;
in joy Lochana tells the glories of Gora.

*Raga sindhura; disha: "A spotless moon has risen in Nadia!"*

**422.** Now here I shall tell a story —
the conversation that took place between Murari and Damodara.[^13-5]

**423.** Damodara Pandita asked Murari:
"I make one request — it is a long-standing pain in my heart.

**424.** Tell me, tell me, Gupta the vaidya, I ask you:
where did Vishvarupa, the Thakura's brother, go?

**425.** With love I ask you about his life."
And Murari, with great joy in his heart, replies:

**426.** "Listen, listen, Damodara, foremost of pandits:
what I know I shall tell you.

**427.** Vishvambhara's elder brother, Vishvarupa, was an abode of virtues;[^13-6]
how can I describe his virtues and deeds!

**428.** In a short time he mastered all the shastras;
his mind was devoted to dharma and averse to worldly life.

**429.** Pure of heart, devoted to brahmanas, gods and guru,
he served his father and mother with great love.

**430.** He knew the conclusions of Vedanta, the essence of every dharma;
without devotion to Vishnu he did no act at all.

**431.** Beloved by all, he had attained the highest perfection;
detached at heart, his mind was fixed in the knowledge of truth.

**432.** One day his father Jagannatha saw him on the main road,
talking with his fellow students, a book in his left hand.

**433.** The son had reached the age of sixteen;
he was fit for marriage, endowed with beauty and youth.

**434.** The father harboured this wish in his heart
and began to consider brides worthy of Vishvarupa.

**435.** Deep in thought, the brahmana came home,
his heart full of one thought: 'I will get Vishvarupa married.'

**436.** Soon the brahmana Vishvarupa also came home,
and, seeing his father lost in thought, he understood what was in his heart.

**437.** So the best of brahmanas, Vishvarupa,
seeing his father lost in thought, understood the matter.

**438.** He understood in his heart: 'It is for my marriage —
they are both anxious how to arrange it.

**439.** For me to marry is not proper;
and if I do not, mother will grieve: it will go against her wish.'

**440.** Having reasoned thus, at daybreak
he went out of the house with a book in his left hand,

**441.** swam across the waters of the Ganga to the other bank,
and, as soon as he had gone, the great soul took sannyasa.

*Raga pathamanjari*

**442.** The third watch of the day has come — why has our son not returned?
The hearts of father and mother are full of anxiety.
Jagannatha searches, looks into house after house,
and did not find his son.

**443.** People whisper to one another, and the matter became known:
Vishvarupa had taken sannyasa.
Hearing the news passed from ear to ear,
the father Jagannatha suddenly lost consciousness.

**444.** Then Shachi-devi too, hearing it, fell senseless to the ground —
the three worlds became dark.
She calls, "Vishvarupa! Come, my son, let me see you!
Why have you renounced the world?

**445.** Such a beautiful body, such beautiful feet —
how will you walk along the roads?
You could not bear the hunger of a single watch —
with whom will you now be fussy?

**446.** When you went to study, son, my heart found no rest —
I kept looking at the time again and again;
when you went to bathe, even then I could not keep still:
'Vishvarupa will come any moment now.'

**447.** When you called me 'Mother', it was wealth of hundreds of thousands to me —
looking at your face I forgot myself.
I do not know what grief befell you that, putting fire into my mouth,[^13-7]
you took sannyasa, a life of poverty.

**448.** Where are you, his father? Go to where Vishvarupa is,
seize our son and bring him home!
Let people say what they will — bring my son and give him to me:
I will invest him with the sacred thread again."

**449.** Jagannatha replies: "Listen, goddess Shachi-rani,
calm your heart.
Grieve no more: all worldly life is vanity;[^13-8]
and Vishvarupa is the best of worthy men.

**450.** It is the good fortune of our line: Vishvarupa, a worthy son,
has taken sannyasa without ever marrying.
Give him this blessing: may he be steady on that path
and keep his sannyasa without hardship.

**451.** Listen: do not regard this as misfortune in the midst of good fortune;
do not grieve without cause.
If one takes sannyasa, millions of generations of the family are delivered —
Vishvarupa is a jewel among men."

**452.** Hearing Jagannatha's words, Shachi-rani speaks again:
"What did you say? Tell me again, my lord.
'If one takes sannyasa, millions of generations are delivered'?
Then my son has done well."

**453.** Thus the two of them, with joy and sorrow in their hearts,
passed some time.
What can I say of that glory? There is no limit to their fortune:
they obtained Vishvambhara as their son.[^13-9]

**454.** So Murari Gupta told, and Damodara Pandita heard,
of Vishvarupa's sannyasa.
Again he asks for the story — the song of Vishvambhara's glories —
says this Lochana Dasa.

**455.** At that time Vishvambhara, sitting on His mother's lap,
gazes into His father's face:
"Wherever My brother has gone — listen, listen, father and mother:
I will take care of you."

**456.** Hearing such words, Jagannatha and Shachi-rani
both together embraced their son;
looking at Vishvambhara's face, they forgot all their sorrows —
so says Lochana Dasa.

*Thus ends the description of the Balya-lila.*

[^13-1]: The numbering of Bhaktisiddhanta's edition goes astray here: after 399 it prints 400, 401, 401 again, then the count returns to 393 and runs 393–403, after which it continues 404, 405… (and so on through the edition). Apparently the compositor was correcting the jump 330 → 340 of portion 12. To keep the edition's numbers, on which all further numbering depends, we retain the printed numbers and distinguish the repeats by a letter: the second "401" is 401a, the repeated series 393–401 is 393a–400a and 401b. The sequence of the couplets (confirmed by the 1983 and 1903 editions) is unchanged.
[^13-2]: Murari, an elder and respected man, prostrated himself before a child; by popular belief, an elder's bow to a younger one brings misfortune on the younger and shortens his life. Hence the parents' alarm and their plea that he bless Nimai with long life.
[^13-3]: Murari Gupta was a vaidya (physician) by caste; the parents respectfully call him a "shudra-muni", a sage who is not a brahmana.
[^13-4]: "Brahman, the Relisher of rasa" is Brahman as the Person who enjoys rasa (cf. Taittiriya Upanishad 2.7: "He, verily, is rasa"), that is, Krishna Himself. Advaita at once discloses to Murari that Nimai is the Lord Himself, but bids him keep it secret.
[^13-5]: Murari Gupta and Damodara Pandita are the narrator and his interlocutor in Murari Gupta's Sanskrit biography (Sri Krishna-chaitanya-charitamrita); Lochana Dasa, who relies on that work, adopts its frame. The story of Vishvarupa is in Murari 1.07.1–10.
[^13-6]: In the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding Vishvarupa is Sankarshana (in Murari, "a portion of Baladeva", 1.07.3). On taking sannyasa he received the name Shankararanya, and he left this world at Pandharpur (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 9.299–300).
[^13-7]: At a cremation it is the son who places fire in the mouth of the dead (mukhagni); Shachi says bitterly that by leaving, her son has as good as performed her funeral rite while she lives.
[^13-8]: That is, attachment to home and family is worthless; this is not a claim that the world is illusory, but a statement of the vanity of worldly life (samsara) beside renunciation for God's sake.
[^13-9]: In A this couplet was lost in the OCR (its number is restored from the neighbouring ones); the text follows the 1903 edition; the 1983 edition ends the stanza: "whose son is Gorachand".

---

# Adi-khanda, part 6 (portion 14)

*The beginning of the Pauganda-lila: Vishvambhara begins to learn His letters; the chudakarana rite; a game on the sandy bank of the Ganga following the tracks of birds; Jagannatha Mishra's anger and Shachi's intercession; Jagannatha's dream: a brahmana reveals to him that his son is the Lord Himself*

## Pauganda-lila (The Boyhood Pastimes)

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* When Gaurasundara, having heard Murari expound the scripture of yoga, began to mock him by imitating his gestures and manners, Murari in anger spoke harsh words to Him. In return, Sriman Mahaprabhu, to show the worthlessness of yoga and what becomes of the yogi, urinated into Murari's plate during his midday meal and instructed him in the supremacy of devotion to Krishna. Then the author, having described how Sriman Mahaprabhu enacted the pastime of sankirtana with the boys of His age, the sannyasa of Vishvarupa as told in a conversation between Murari and Damodara, the grief of Shachi and Jagannatha and so on, proceeds to describe Gaurasundara's Pauganda-lila. In due time the chudakarana and other rites of Gaurahari were duly performed. As prescribed, at an auspicious moment chalk was placed in His hand;[^14-1] He […][^14-2] Mishra Purandara, learning [in a dream] that [his son] is Bhagavan, was enlightened; but when this mood of his […], he became absorbed once more in his parental love (vatsalya). At the appointed time Gaurahari's upanayana rite was performed according to rule. Then the teaching on the avataras of the four yugas is set out. At the end of the Dvapara-yuga, Bhagavan Himself, Sri Krishna, the son of the king of Vraja, appears. In the Kali-yuga, assuming the feelings and the lustre of Srimati Radha, He descends in a hidden way in the form of Sri Gaurasundara to preach the dharma of the age — the sankirtana of Hari's name. Intoxicated with prema, He gave prema away, offering it at the door of every living being. During His life as a householder, by forbidding His mother to eat grains on the day of Ekadashi, He taught the living beings of the world. When Jagannatha Mishra fell ill, He instructed about the impermanence of human life and comforted His mother in many ways. When Jagannatha Mishra passed from this world, Shachi-devi mourned her husband; Gaurahari too mourned His father. After that He began His studies […].[^14-3]

*Raga dhanashi*

**457.** Thus one day Mishra Purandara,
looking at Vishvambhara, began to reflect.

**458.** On an auspicious day, at an auspicious moment, under a good tithi and nakshatra,
he placed chalk in His hand[^14-1] — a wonderful occasion it was.

**459.** Day by day He studies, the Guru of the world;
seeing this, Shachi and Jagannatha forget themselves.

**459+1.** How sweetly the Lord says "ka, kha, ga, gha"![^14-4]
Seeing this, Shachi and Jagannatha forget every sorrow.

**459+2.** In two or three days He learned to write all the letters with all their signs;
unceasingly He writes out garlands of Krishna's names:

**459+3.** "Rama, Krishna, Govinda, Gopala, Vanamali" —
day and night He writes and reads them with delight.

**460.** Thus some days passed in play,
and Jagannatha and Shachi both took counsel together.

**461.** Having resolved in their minds on Vishvambhara's chudakarana,[^14-5]
they then invited all their friends and kinsfolk.

**462.** Having considered the auspicious moment, tithi and day,
they made their decision firm: "We shall perform the chudakarana."

**463.** In the town of Nadia every house rejoiced;
they invited brahmanas and worthy men honoured by all.

**464.** The brahmanas recite the Vedas, the singers sing songs;
the sacrificial rite was performed as was proper.

**465.** All the respectable women raise the cry "Jaya, jaya!";
to everyone were given perfumes, betel nut and sandal.

**466.** All kinds of instruments sound — the joy is boundless:
conches, dundubhis, bheur and kahal horns resound;

**467.** mridangas, pataha drums and bronze cymbals resound;
the sound of the sanai is sweetness itself to hear.

**468.** On all sides cries of "Hari!" fill the sky;
then the chudakarana and the ear-piercing were performed.

**469.** The whole town of Nadia rejoiced:
seeing Vishvambhara's face, people forget themselves.

**470.** In the market, on the road, at the ghat — wherever anyone goes,
when two meet, they sing of Gorachand's glories.

**471.** Seeing another's son, this is what they feel in their hearts:
"It is Shachi and Jagannatha's good fortune — such a son!

**472.** It is the good fortune of Navadvipa and the good fortune of the world;
whoever sees that form — his eyes are worthy of praise."

**472+1.** Hearing such words, everyone rejoices;
with bliss in his heart Lochana Dasa tells this.[^14-6]

**473.** On another day, on the sandy bank of the Ganga,
He is playing with the boys at the ghat.

**474.** Following the tracks of birds on the sand,
He walked, stepping exactly in the birds' footprints.

**475.** Saying this,[^14-7] Mahaprabhu Sri Gaurachandra
set a rule for the game with the boys:

**476.** "Whichever boy steps beyond these footprints
loses the game at once.

**477.** Whoever can run ahead and catch [another]
wins the game and rides on his shoulders.

**478.** Riding on his shoulders, he whips his back with a switch,
and the other carries him on his shoulders to the agreed ghat."

**479.** Saying this, He runs over the sand with the children;
from great exertion sweat streams down His body.

**480.** At that time Jagannatha Mishra Purandara
went to the bank of the Jahnavi to bathe.

**481.** Seeing his son's game, he grew angry;
seeing Him so worn out, the father's heart began to burn:

**482.** it was as if a golden lotus had wilted in the heat,
and the sweat on His face was like honey oozing out.

**483.** Calling out again and again, Mishra follows Him;
seeing His father, Vishvambhara was greatly abashed.

**484.** Out of shame He does not raise His face, fear in His heart;
the pandit himself went up to Gorachand,

**485.** took Him by the hand and led his son away;
all the boys went off to their homes.

**486.** Having bathed in the Ganga, Jagannatha came home
and, on coming home, scolded Gorachand at length:

**487.** "All Your studies are ruined, as with some good-for-nothing!
Why, with Your foolish mind, do You roam about all the time?

**488.** You are a brahmana's son, yet You have no proper conduct —
I am giving You now the fruit You deserve."

**489.** Saying this, Jagannatha took a switch in his hand;
as he threatened, Shachi caught hold of his hand:

**490.** "Do not beat my son! He will not play any more;
He will always study, sitting beside you."

**491.** Vishvambhara hid in His mother's lap
and says very softly, "I won't play, I won't play."

**492.** Pushing Jagannatha back and shielding her son,
[Shachi says:] "Do not beat my son — He is dying of fright!"

**493.** Saying this, Shachi-devi took her son on her lap
and wiped His face with the end of her sari.

**494.** "Let my son not study, let Him be a dunce —
as long as, a dunce, He lives a hundred years!"

**495.** Hearing Shachi's words, Mishra Purandara
began to answer her in anger:

**496.** "If our son is a dunce, how will He make a living?
What brahmana will give Him a daughter in marriage?"

**497.** Then Jagannatha looked at his son's face:
the son looks at his father with frightened eyes.

**498.** Mishra's heart burns, though outwardly he is stern —
and, overcome by love, he threw away the switch.

**499.** With tears in his eyes he took his son on his lap
and gently persuades Him with sweet words:

**500.** "Of one who studies and listens to his lessons, my boy, people speak well;
and I will give You a silk dhoti and bananas."

**501.** Thus in joy and gladness the day went by;
having finished his evening rites, Mishra went to bed.

**502.** He fell asleep, and in the third watch of the night
Mishra had a dream and was thrown into turmoil.

**503.** Rising at daybreak, he called everyone:
"I have seen a dream — I will tell you all.

**504.** I saw a brahmana of majestic stature,
radiant like the rays of the sun.

**505.** His divine body was adorned with jewels;
I could not look at him — the whole house was ablaze.

**506.** In a voice deep as thunder he said to me:
'Why do you regard Vishvambhara as your son?

**507.** I am God, Narayana — and you do not know it;
why do you regard [Me] merely as your son?[^14-8]

**508.** A beast does not know the touch of the philosopher's stone;
you take Me for your son — but for you that is only natural.[^14-9]

**509.** I know all the shastras, I am the Guru of all the gods;
why then do you take a switch in hand to teach Me?'

**510.** Such a dream I have seen today,
and ever since, I do not know what is happening in my heart."

**511.** Shachi was overjoyed, and so were all the people;
all gaze at Vishvambhara's face.

**512.** Shachi and Jagannatha embrace Him, pressing Him to their hearts:
"Our son is Vishvambhara Gaurahari!

**513.** He whose boundless glory the Vedas do not know,
whom Shiva, Sanaka and the others do not attain in meditation —

**514.** who can know the greatness of this Greatest One?
Lord Gaura has been born as my son!"

**515.** But even as they spoke, tender parental love (vatsalya) awoke in them,
and every feeling of His majesty withdrew.[^14-10]

**516.** Hearing of the dream, all the people rejoice —
the glories of Gora are joyfully sung by this Lochana Dasa.

[^14-1]: "Hate khadi", "chalk in the hand": the rite of beginning to learn letters (vidyarambha), when the child first traces letters with chalk.
[^14-2]: Here in the OCR of the summary the lines are overlaid by text from a neighbouring page (vv. 416–418), and part of the sentence is lost; in substance it concerns the games with the boys, the father's anger (vv. 473–500) and Jagannatha's dream (vv. 501–516).
[^14-3]: The name or word before "began His studies" is unreadable in the OCR.
[^14-4]: Vv. 459+1 – 459+3 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition does not have them. "Ka, kha, ga, gha" are the first letters of the Bengali alphabet; "with all their signs" is literally "all the phalas", the signs for conjunct consonants.
[^14-5]: Chudakarana is the samskara of the first cutting of the hair, leaving a lock (shikha) on the crown.
[^14-6]: V. 472+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition does not have it, but gives in a footnote, from one manuscript, a different signature: "Meditating on the lotus feet of Narahari, Lochana joyfully sings of Chaitanya."
[^14-7]: That is, announcing this game; the 1983 edition has "thus".
[^14-8]: In the dream the brahmana speaks in the person of the Lord Himself. In Murari (1.07.14–16) the brahmana speaks to Jagannatha about his son, and Mishra replies that even if his son is Narayana, it is a father's duty to correct Him.
[^14-9]: So A ("e bada sahaja"). The 1983 edition reads "e bada sahasa": "this is great audacity".
[^14-10]: Cf. the Bhagavata (10.8.43–45): Yashoda, having seen the universe in Krishna's mouth, is at once covered again by His yogamaya and sees in Him only her son. The parental love (vatsalya) of Shachi and Jagannatha does not admit the thought of the Lord's majesty, and He Himself by His own will hides it for the sake of their love.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 7 (portion 15)

*Pauganda-lila (continued): Vishvambhara's upanayana; during the rite He is overcome by the ecstasy of prema, and the astonished pandits reason about the avataras for a special purpose and the yuga-avataras, about Krishna in the Dvapara and Gaura in the Kali, about Radha and the dharma of service, and conclude that Vishvambhara is the Lord Himself*

*Raga varadi; disha: "Gorachand is my life!"*

**517.** Thus in joy and gladness the days pass;
the town of Nadia floats in an ocean of happiness.

**518.** Who can tell the happiness of a single moment!
The good fortune of Shachi and Jagannatha cannot be contained in the universe.

**519.** One day Jagannatha suddenly saw
his well-mannered son among His companions.

**520.** The son is in His ninth year — a fitting and auspicious time;
"I shall give Him the sacred thread," he thought in his heart.

**521.** Coming home, he took counsel with Shachi,
called an astrologer and fixed an auspicious day.

**522.** Gathering friends and kinsfolk, he announced to them:
"Give me leave to invest Vishvambhara with the sacred thread."

**523.** Mishra engaged as acharya a famous pandit
who knows the rites of sacrifice and knows the Vedic ordinances.

**524.** To the brahmanas were given betel nuts, sandal and garlands;
hundreds of respectable women put on sindura.

**525.** Puffed rice, bananas, oil and turmeric
the virtuous Shachi gave to each and every one.

**526.** Conches sound, the women's cries of "Jaya, jaya!" ring out;
at the hour of dusk they perform the rite of anointing with fragrances (adhivasa).[^15-1]

**527.** The brahmanas recite the Vedas, the bhat bards their eulogies;
giving blessings, they did all according to rule and custom.

**528.** Rising at daybreak, Mishra Purandara
performed the nandimukha-shraddha beautifully.[^15-2]

**529.** Having honoured the brahmanas with water for the feet and for sipping,
judging the time had come, he began the sacrificial rite.

**530.** Here, meanwhile, Shachi-devi with all the married women
goes about making merry at her son's festival.

**530+1.** All the nagaris, as many as there were, surrounded Gauranga[^15-3]
and wished to anoint His holy body.

**531.** They anointed Vishvambhara's body with oil and turmeric,
washed His head with fragrant amalaki

**532.** and bathed Him with water of the heavenly river;
Shachi forgets herself in waves of joy.

**533.** Conches, dundubhis, bheur and kahal horns resound;
mridangas, patahas and bronze cymbals resound.

**534.** The rumble of the dhak drums is heard a yojana away;
at the sound of the sahini the heart is gladdened.

**535.** Vinas, flutes, kapilasa horns, pipes, nisan drums,
rababs, upangas and pakhoyajes play in unison.

**536.** Dancers dance, singers sing songs;
at an auspicious moment His head was shaved.

**537.** Every limb of His was adorned with ornaments,
and He was dressed in perfumes, garlands and sandal.

**538.** Shachi's son was brought to the place of sacrifice,
where the host of brahmanas chant the Vedas.

**539.** They clothed Him in red garments and the sacred thread;
seeing His beauty, Ananga (Kamadeva) himself was entranced.

**540.** His father speaks the mantra into Vishvambhara's ear;
seeing the staff in His hand, sin itself trembled with fear.

**541.** The Lord begs alms — such is the custom of this ashrama;
the sannyasa ashrama is the essence of all ashramas.

**542.** It was in His mind: "For the dharma of the age I shall take sannyasa,"
and at the time His head was shaved, He remembered it.[^15-4]

**543.** "So I shall do," and ecstasy seized Him:
"Wandering, I shall dispel the sufferings of all the people of Kali!"

**544.** His whole body thrills from head to foot;
each single bristling hair surpasses the filaments of the kadamba.

**545.** His two long eyes are compassionate and reddish;
the radiance of His body is like the rising sun.

**546.** At the onset of prema — a great, proud, thundering roar;
seeing this, all the brahmanas were astounded.

**547.** Sudarshana[^15-5] and the other leading pandits,
gathering together, reason among themselves:

**548.** all the pandits, meeting together, deliberate:
"This son of Shachi is not a human being.

**549.** This is the effulgence of some god," they understood with certainty;
"such effulgence can belong to none but Govinda.

**550.** What do we know of the Lord's deeds and conduct?
We only conjecture by the reasoning of our intellect."

**551.** One said: "Listen to my words:
the Lord's deeds we do not understand — that is certain.

**552.** Listen to what I say, as I myself understand it:
to deliver the people, the Lord takes birth age after age.

**553.** There are many avataras, according to the purpose for which they come;
but according to the nature of the yugas there are only four avataras.[^15-6]

**554.** To establish dharma, to destroy adharma
and to deliver the righteous, He appears.

**555.** Those who come to slay the asuras and the like
are called avataras for a special purpose (karya-avataras).

**556.** All the avataras that are written of, beginning with Sri Rama,
are karya-avataras: their deeds bear witness to it.

**557.** In the Treta-yuga [the yuga-avatara] is red, its dharma is sacrifice;
the Lord, dark as a blade of durva grass, [came] to destroy the rakshasas.

**558.** Not in every Treta does Raghunatha appear
to play with the monkeys in order to slay Ravana.

**559.** Ravana's lifespan is fourteen chatur-yugas;
count, brothers, how many Tretas went by!

**560.** That is why I say: not all Tretas are the same —
[the avataras come] according to purpose, depending on what happens when.

**561.** In the Satya-yuga [the avatara] is white, the dharma is austerity, the name Hamsa;
Nrisimha and the others I consider karya-avataras.

**562.** He who, with a colour befitting the yuga, establishes [its] dharma —
him I know as the avatara of the yuga (yuga-avatara).

**563.** Listen attentively about Krishna in the Dvapara:
He alone is the Lord, and no one else.

**564.** Whether karya-avatara or yuga-avatara —
the son of Nanda is full of all the parts (kalas).

**565.** Him all call the full, complete Brahman;
know that in Vrindavana He is the One who longs for the gopis' love.[^15-7]

**566.** The avatara of Krishna is the crown of all avataras;
among the Dvaparas this Dvapara is the best.

**567.** In other Dvaparas there are two [kinds of] avataras:
either a karya-avatara or a yuga-avatara.

**568.** In the Kali-yuga that follows the Dvapara in which Krishna descends,
Gaurachand appears.[^15-8]

**569.** As is the Krishna-avatara, so is Gaurachandra;
these two yugas are distinct from all the yugas.

**570.** Not in every Dvapara are there Krishna's pastimes,
not in every Kali-yuga does Gora descend.

**571.** Many Dvaparas, Kalis, Satyas and Tretas pass —
in all of them the Lord appears as partial avataras.

**572.** In this Dvapara and in this Kali-yuga,
by great fortune, one meets Krishna and Krishna Chaitanya.

**573.** In one day of Brahma He descends once
and sports in the Dvapara and in the Kali-yuga.

**574.** In the manvantara of Vaivasvata, becoming dark and golden,
He performed the worship (service), [the dharma] of the Dvapara, and [in the Kali] kirtana.

**575.** Blessed, blessed is the Kali-yuga — above all the yugas:
in the sacrifice of sankirtana all became qualified to take part.

**576.** O, O merciful Lord Gaurachandra!
By sankirtana He carried across [the ocean of the world] the lame, the dull and the blind.

**577.** If you do not trust my words,
then answer properly what I shall ask.

**578.** Whatever colour and dharma belong to a yuga,
that work the Lord performs as the avatara of the yuga.

**579.** In the Dvapara the Lord Krishna was the avatara of the yuga:
how then did He carry out the dharma of the age?

**580.** The shastras say that in the Dvapara the dharma is service (paricharya);
where did the Lord establish this dharma?

**581.** If you will not disdain it, I shall say one thing;
I speak with reason — do not reject my words.

**582.** He Himself is the Lord, the independent Ishvara;
both the special purpose and the dharma of the age are all in His hands.

**583.** The establishment of the dharma of the age and every special purpose —
all this the Lord accomplished; it is wonderful to comprehend!

**584.** To sport by descending as Radha-Krishna,
He Himself is the independent [Lord], and Radha is in the form of [His] prakriti.[^15-9]

**585.** Like prakriti and purusha, They are soul and body;
both are one body, but, understanding the purpose [of the pastimes], They became two.

**586.** She bears the name Radha because Her task is the worship (aradhana) of Krishna;
She serves Him together with the society of the gopis.

**587.** The gopis, hundreds upon hundreds of branches, serve with prema and devotion;
but the very essence of [His] prakriti is Radhika alone.

**588.** She offers [Him] Her senses, Her body, the very nature of Her body;
and in this Her ever-new love (anuraga) grows.

**589.** No one understood this dharma of service;
so say all the bhagavatas.[^15-10]

**590.** In other Dvapara-yugas He acts through a portion
and establishes dharma, [but people] do not grasp its essence.

**591.** Charity, vows and austerity are called dharma,
and all, having performed dharma, offer [its fruit] to Him.

**592.** That is why the Lord revealed Himself in person;
and still no one understood the root of dharma and adharma.

**593.** In the Kali-yuga He reveals Himself in a golden body
and, doing the work of the yuga-avatara, reveals prema.

**594.** His body has Radha's complexion — He became golden,
holding within His heart Radhika's feelings and rasa.

**595.** In that mood He weeps, the crown of the relishers of rasa;
His body is like a blossoming kadamba, all thrilling.

**596.** Intoxicated with that prema, brimming over with it,
He roars and thunders, weeping and weeping.

**597.** Hearing that roar, the age of Kali that lay senseless —
all regained consciousness, and the joy was immense.

**598.** Therefore, crying "Radha-Krishna", they dance, weep and laugh;
the darkness went away and they found light.

**599.** Krishna, born in the Dvapara, [now] in a golden body
awakened the senseless people of the Kali.

**600.** The Lord reveals prema, assuming a humble guise;
He gives Himself away and counts it His own gain.

**601.** What lord ever showed such lordship?
He gives prema even to those who do not worship Him — without discrimination.

**602.** That is why I say: this is the avatara of the yuga;
and He also manifested this full avatara.

**603.** In other Kali-yugas, however, there is an avatara of Narayana;
His name is Krishna, as in the Dvapara-yuga.[^15-11]

**604.** His colour surpasses a parrot's wing —
"the lustre of sapphire", says the commentator.

**605.** In this Kali-yuga Gaurachandra is the full Brahman,
and the portion [— the yuga-avatara —] entered into Him: this is the essence I have told.[^15-12]

**606.** Chaitanya Gosani is the fullest of full avataras;
there is no one else who is such an ocean of compassion.

**607.** Karya-avatara and yuga-avatara are one in Him;
therefore, in accordance with the yuga, Gaura is manifest before our eyes.

**608.** The shastras say: in the Kali [the avatara] is golden, the dharma is sankirtana;[^15-13]
this is the Lord Vishvambhara, and no one else."

**609.** Having reflected, all the pandits became firm in heart;
and the Lord, understanding the time, restrained Himself.

**610.** Then in a moment the Lord withdrew it all,
and Vishvambhara Gaurahari spoke [as usual].

**611.** All the people whisper to one another about the wondrous event;
guessing this way and that, they go off here and there.

**612.** Some remained in amazement, with doubt in their hearts:
"What have we seen? What lies behind Vishvambhara's deeds?"

**613.** What they had heard from people about Vishvambhara
they saw with their own eyes: He is the Creator of the world.

**614.** The town overflowed with joy, all cry "Jaya, jaya!";
the blessed song of Gora's glories is sung by this Lochana.

[^15-1]: Adhivasa is a preparatory rite on the eve of a samskara, with anointing with fragrances and the distribution of auspicious gifts.
[^15-2]: The nandimukha-shraddha is an offering to the ancestors performed before a samskara (upanayana, wedding and the like).
[^15-3]: V. 530+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition gives its first line in a footnote as a variant of v. 531. On Lochana Dasa's nagari-bhava see en/09, note 9.
[^15-4]: At the upanayana the brahmachari student receives a staff and begs alms like a sannyasi; Lochana Dasa sees in this a foreshadowing of the sannyasa the Lord will take in 1510.
[^15-5]: Sudarshana Pandita was one of the Lord's teachers in Navadvipa (in Murari, 1.09.1).
[^15-6]: Lochana Dasa's division: "karya-avataras" (avataras for a special purpose — slaying asuras and the like; in the Chaitanya-charitamrita the lila-avataras correspond to them) and "yuga-avataras", who establish the dharma of their age in the colour proper to the yuga (cf. Bhagavata 11.5.20–32).
[^15-7]: Literally "gopika-lampata", "avid for the gopis". In the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding this is Krishna's transcendental love for the gopis, His internal energy, which has nothing in common with worldly lust.
[^15-8]: Cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita (Adi 3.6–10): Krishna descends once in a day of Brahma, at the end of the Dvapara of the twenty-eighth chatur-yuga of the Vaivasvata manvantara, and in the Kali following it appears as Sri Chaitanya. Verses 573–574 say the same.
[^15-9]: "Prakriti" here is not material nature but the Lord's internal energy (hladini-shakti), personified by Radha; Radha and Krishna are one soul in two bodies (cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 4.55–56, 4.96–98).
[^15-10]: "Bhagavatas" are the Lord's devotees; the reading "so says the Bhagavata" is also possible.
[^15-11]: That is, in ordinary Kali-yugas the yuga-avatara is "krishna-varna", dark in colour (cf. Bhagavata 11.5.32 and Sridhara Svami's comment: "shining like sapphire"). The line is corrupt in A; the reading of the 1983 edition is adopted; its exact sense is unclear.
[^15-12]: Cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita (Adi 4.8–12): the dharma of the age is established by a portion of the Lord, the yuga-avatara; but when Krishna Himself descends, all the avataras, the yuga-avatara included, enter into Him.
[^15-13]: "Pita" means yellow, golden; cf. Bhagavata (10.8.13): Garga Muni speaks of the three former colours of the avataras — white, red and yellow (pita) — and the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in "yellow" an indication of Gauranga.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 8 (portion 16)

*Pauganda-lila (continued): Vishvambhara tells His mother not to eat grains on Ekadashi and for a moment becomes motionless after saying "I am going"; the conversation of Damodara and Murari Gupta on how the Lord abides in the body of His devotee; the illness and departure of Jagannatha Mishra on the bank of the Ganga, the grief of Shachi and of the Lord, the funeral rites*

*Raga shri; disha: "O, what do we see! Glory, glory to Gauranga!" Refrain: "What nectar-bliss is the prema of my Gauranga! What a Gauranga is mine! Glory, glory!"*

**615.** Another day the Lord, sitting in His house,
revealed what was in His heart.

**616.** His whole body was filled with the nectar of His own effulgence;
one could not look at it — the house was all ablaze with light.

**617.** Seeing His mother, He said: "Listen to what I say:
I have noticed one great fault in you.

**618.** Do not eat grains any more on the day of Ekadashi;[^16-1]
carefully keep this word of Mine."

**619.** In a voice deep as the rumbling of clouds He said this to His mother;
hearing it, the mother was amazed, and reverent awe filled her heart.

**620.** Her body filled with shyness, awe and love;
"I shall keep Your command," she said very softly.

**621.** Hearing His mother's words, He, kind at heart,
with a gladdened heart taught her dharma.

**622.** At that time a brahmana suddenly came
and with a pure heart brought Him betel leaf and nut.

**623.** Smiling, the Lord then ate the betel nut,
and a moment later called His mother again.

**624.** The Lord said to His mother: "I am going; [this] body —
guard it carefully: it is your own son."[^16-2]

**625.** Saying this, He remained motionless for half a moment
and lay stretched on the ground like a stick.

**626.** Again He lay silent; Shachi, terrified,
hastily pours Ganga water into His mouth.

**627.** In a moment the Lord came to Himself,
and the house was lit up by the effulgence of His natural form.

**628.** The Lord said to His mother: "I am going; the body…" —
is there anyone who can judge this matter?

**629.** Sri Murari Gupta the vaidya, intimate with the Lord,
a knower of every truth, a seasoned devotee —

**630.** Damodara Pandita asked him:
"Great soul, tell me the truth of this.

**631.** Did the Lord display [His] yogamaya,[^16-3] or some power?
Consider it and give me a reasoned answer."

**632.** Murari says: "Listen, listen, good sir:
do I know all the intentions of Krishna?

**633.** I shall say something as my own reason suggests;
if it proves well founded, keep it in your heart.

**634.** When they hear [of Him], see, meditate and sing sankirtana,
the Lord enters the hearts of His devotees.

**635.** His own body is not a [material] body: it is a form beyond the gunas;[^16-4]
the enjoyment of the gunas, conduct and deliberation [belong to those] in the gunas.

**636.** Therefore He accepts the devotee's body as [His] body
and there freely plays in all His doings.

**637.** He regards the worship of His devotee as greater than worship of Himself;
within Himself He knows that in this lies the fullness of all worship.

**638.** The Lord Himself is the very one who depends on Him;
only in worldly usage, by maya, are they spoken of as "two".[^16-5]

**639.** Why He regards the devotee as greater than Himself —
this the whole world cannot understand.

**640.** A form full of rasa, a body full of beauty,
a body endowed with every splendour — so He has manifested Himself.

**641.** He has nothing but play, sport and lila;
what wretch reviles Him by calling Him "without qualities"?

**642.** By His maya He does not openly manifest Himself[^16-6]
and plays unceasingly in the body of His devotee.

**643.** The devotee's eating, sleeping, resting and enjoyment —
in these very things Krishna's happiness becomes manifest.

**644.** The conduct of devotees and of other people is the same;
by the nature of the body they look the same to the eye.

**645.** Those who, seeing with their eyes, take [them] for men
[say]: 'Where is Krishna? With my eyes I see a man.

**646.** Krishna, the Lord of all lords, the Brahman beyond the gunas —
can He be doing mundane deeds in a human body?'

**647.** Saying this, the low man does not accept [it];
but the elevated one knows the Lord's body in the devotee's body.[^16-7]

**648.** Such is my reasoning, this is how I think;
understand for yourself and decide in your heart what is right.

**649.** Know that a Vaishnava's body is always full of Krishna;
so we hear in the Vedas, the Puranas and the Bhagavata.

**650.** By the dust of his feet all people are purified;
he purifies even the Ganga and all the other holy places.

**651.** The low man who disputes about the body of such a person
commits an offence out of incomprehension."

**652.** Thus Damodara and Murari Gupta
concluded their talk, both with gladdened hearts.

**653.** The Lord does not count His own body as a body,
but regards the body of His devotee as His own.

**654.** Having reasoned thus, the two departed;
hearing of it, this Lochana Dasa speaks with joy.

*Raga vibhasa; disha: "Ah, ah!" Refrain: "Ah, ah, my life!"*

**655.** Listen, everyone, to another wondrous account;
when you hear it, the pain in your heart will be dispelled.

**656.** Having learned the whole essence of the Vedas in his teacher's ashrama,
Jagannatha, the jewel of brahmanas, came home.[^16-8]

**657.** By the will of providence a fever seized his body;
seeing the grievous fever, all were struck with fear.

**658.** Seeing Shachi weeping in utter distress,
the Lord consoles her, explaining the truth:

**659.** "Mother, death is certain for all:
for Brahma and Rudra, the oceans, the mountains and the Himalaya,[^16-9]

**660.** Indra, Varuna, Agni — time destroys them all;
why are you so afraid of death?

**661.** Call all your kinsfolk and friends now:
let them all together help [him] remember the name of Krishna."

**662.** Knowing that at the time of death this is truly the duty of kin,
the Lord, the divine Jewel of the Yadus, has [His father] helped to remember [Krishna].

**663.** Hearing this, all the kinsfolk and friends came;
coming to the Lord's house, they surrounded Mishra.

**664.** Those friends who were mature in judgement,
seeing that the hour was near, took counsel.

**665.** Vishvambhara says: "Mother, why do you delay?
All our near ones and kin are needed at once."

**666.** Saying this, mother and son lifted him up
and with their kinsfolk went to the bank of the Jahnavi.

**667.** Clasping His father's feet, Vishvambhara weeps;
He cannot hold back His tears, His voice falters:

**668.** "Leaving Me, father, where are you going?
Never again shall I call anyone 'Father!'

**669.** From today this house of Mine is empty;
never again shall I see your feet, father.

**670.** Today all ten directions are empty, all is pitch dark:
no one will teach Me so lovingly, holding Me by the hand."

**671.** Hearing such nectarean words,
Jagannatha in his anguish cannot utter a word.

**672.** In a faltering voice he says: "Listen, Vishvambhara:
what was in my heart cannot be told.

**673.** I have entrusted You to the feet of Raghunatha;[^16-10]
only may You never at any time forget me."

**674.** Saying this, he gives himself to remembrance: "Hari, Hari!"
All the brahmanas lowered him into the water of the Ganga.[^16-11]

**675.** They placed a garland of tulasi around his neck;
on all sides kinsfolk and friends chant the name of Hari.

**676.** On all sides resounds the sankirtana of Hari's qualities;
and at that moment the best of brahmanas went to Vaikuntha.[^16-12]

**677.** Mounting a chariot, the brahmana departed for Vaikuntha;
at Shachi's wailing the earth is rent asunder.

**678.** Clasping her husband's feet, she weeps, rolling on the ground:
"I shall go! Take me with you!

**679.** For so long I served you;
you have gone to Vaikuntha, and I am left on the earth.

**680.** I served you at your rest and at your meals;
today all ten directions are empty, all is pitch dark.

**681.** I am left forsaken with your younger son;
where will Nimai live, to whose face will He look?

**682.** Your son Nimai, rare in all the world —
forgetting it all, you are going, my lord!"

**683.** Seeing His mother's weeping and His father's death,
Shachi's son weeps, tears streaming from His eyes.

**684.** Like a pearl necklace strung on a thread,
the tears flow from His eyes onto His broad chest.

**685.** Devotees and friends cry out, "Alas, alas!";
at the Lord's weeping the whole world weeps.

**686.** All soothed [Him] with sweet words:
"Lord, at Your weeping creation perishes!"

**687.** The women consoled Shachi-devi:
"Looking at Vishvambhara, Shachi, you will forget everything."

**688.** The Lord, steadfast Himself, restraining everything within,
performed the rites befitting the time.

**689.** Then, surrounded by His kinsfolk, He performed His father's rites
as the Vedic injunctions prescribe.

**690.** The Lord, loving His father, performed the offering to the ancestors
and, in due order and according to rule, honoured the brahmanas.

**691.** Water vessels, rice, food and all other gifts
the Lord, devoted to His father, gave to the brahmanas.

**692.** This is the account of Jagannatha's going to Vaikuntha —
of that best of brahmanas who was the father of Vishvambhara Himself.

**693.** If a person hears this account with faith,
he goes to Vaikuntha, as one who dies at the Ganga.

**694.** Looking at Gorachand, Shachi sighs:
"May the fatherless son not fall into despair!

**695.** If His heart becomes immersed in the taste of learning,
then my son will pass His days happily."

**696.** Listen, everyone, to this wondrous account:
Lochana tells something of the deeds of Chaitanya.

[^16-1]: Ekadashi is the eleventh day of each lunar fortnight, a day of fasting; "anna" means grain (rice and the like). Bhaktisiddhanta's summary (en/14) stresses that by forbidding His mother to eat grain on Ekadashi the Lord taught the whole world. In Murari — 1.07.18–20.
[^16-2]: In Murari (1.07.21) the Lord says: "I am going. Guard the body of your son, which will for half a moment become motionless." The conversation of Damodara and Murari (vv. 629–653) interprets exactly these words.
[^16-3]: Literally "maya". In Murari (1.07.24, 27) the same question concerns the Lord's "yogamaya": when said of the Lord, maya is His internal power, yogamaya.
[^16-4]: "Nirguna" here means beyond the material gunas, not "devoid of form and qualities": in vv. 640–641 Lochana Dasa expressly condemns those who call the Lord "nirguna" in an impersonal sense. In v. 640 "niramila" literally means "made": the Lord Himself manifests His eternal form.
[^16-5]: The line also admits the reading: "He Himself is the Lord, and that one (the devotee) depends on Him." From the sense of the whole argument (vv. 624, 653), Lochana Dasa is explaining the words "I am going; the body is your son": in Gaura the Lord Himself plays the part of His own devotee, and that people see "two" here is the working of His maya (yogamaya). The eternal distinction between the Lord and the devotee (vv. 637, 639) is not denied by this.
[^16-6]: Here maya is the power by which the Lord conceals Himself, that is, yogamaya.
[^16-7]: The reasoning of vv. 634–653 follows Murari (1.08.1–7): the Lord enters the heart of the devotee and regards the devotee's body as His own. In the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding (Bhaktisiddhanta), the Lord's body is not different from Himself and is not material, and Mahaprabhu is Krishna Himself who has assumed the form and feeling of His own devotee (bhakta-rupa); what is said of the "devotee's body" therefore does not mean that in Gaura the Lord uses a separate, human body.
[^16-8]: The subject of the line is Jagannatha Mishra; cf. Murari (1.08.13): the Lord's father too studied Vedanta and other scriptures.
[^16-9]: According to the Puranas, Brahma and Rudra, who govern the universe, are also not eternal in these offices: at the end of Brahma's life the universe is dissolved.
[^16-10]: Raghunatha ("Lord of the Raghu dynasty") is Sri Ramachandra; Jagannatha says the same words in Murari (1.08.17).
[^16-11]: The custom of antarjali: the dying person is carried to the bank of the Ganga and immersed waist-deep in its water so that he may leave the body remembering Hari.
[^16-12]: Jagannatha Mishra is an eternal associate of the Lord (in the Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika — Nanda Maharaja and Vasudeva); his departure is not an ordinary death, and Lochana Dasa speaks of it as leaving for Vaikuntha on a chariot.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 9 (portion 17)

*Pauganda-lila (conclusion): Shachi entrusts her son to the pandits; studies under Vishnu Pandita, Sudarshana and Gangadasa; the first meeting with Vallabhacharya's daughter on the way to the Ganga; Vanamali Acharya's matchmaking — Shachi's refusal, the matchmaker's lament, his meeting with the Lord and Shachi's consent; Vallabhacharya gladly agrees to give his daughter*

*Raga dhanashi; disha: "Ah, ah!"*

**697.** One day Shachi, taking Gaurahari by the hand,
put Gauranga to His studies.

**698.** Entrusting her son to all the pandits,
the goddess speaks humbly and plaintively:

**699.** "Teach my son, good sirs;
keep Him close to you, do not keep Him at a distance.

**700.** My son has lost His father — be kind to Him;
regard Him as your own son."

**701.** Hearing this, all the pandits, abashed at heart,
began to answer humbly:

**702.** "Today we have come to know our good fortune:
we have obtained the Lord of millions of Sarasvatis.[^17-1]

**703.** He will teach the whole world His own prema and His name;
He is the teacher of all people, the foremost of all.

**704.** It is we who shall learn in His presence —
know this for certain, mother; we have told you the truth."

**705.** Hearing this, Shachi-devi answered with humble words
and, having entrusted her son, returned to her home.

**706.** Then, after a few more days, the Lord Vishvambhara
went to study at the house of Vishnu Pandita.

**707.** Under Sudarshana and the pandit Gangadasa
the Teacher of the world studied — for their own good.

**708.** He who by His yogamaya has manifested a human form[^17-2] behaves like people;
out of kindness to people He studies and teaches knowledge.

**709.** One day, at the school of Pandita Sri Sudarshana,
the Lord jokes with His fellow students.

**710.** He talks in the speech of the East Bengalis — most amusingly;[^17-3]
His laughter, full of nectar, captivates the heart.

**711.** Thus some days passed in merry pranks;
He decided to visit Vanamali Acharya.

**712.** To see him He went to his house;
seeing [Him], he rose in reverence and bowed.

**713.** Taking him by the hand, He walks with him along the road,
talking of amusing and confidential things.

**714.** At that time the daughter of Vallabha Acharya,
famed in the three worlds for beauty, virtue and character,

**715.** is going with her friends to bathe in the Ganga;
Vishvambhara Hari suddenly sees her.

**716.** The Lord gazes at her steadily with a smiling face
and, seeing her, knew why she had been born.[^17-4]

**717.** Lakshmi Thakurani understood this from the sign,
and the goddess took the lotus feet of the Lord upon her head.[^17-5]

**718.** Acharya Vanamali was very shrewd:
he understood what was in their hearts — the sprouting feeling.

**719.** The next day Vanamali Acharya himself,
with joy in his heart, went to Shachi's house.

**720.** Smiling, he bowed at Shachi's feet
and, bowing, spoke with sweet words:

**721.** "There is a girl worthy of your son:
for beauty, virtue and character she is famed in the three worlds.

**722.** She is the daughter of Vallabha Acharya, most well-mannered;
if you have the wish, tell me what is in your heart."

**723.** Hearing the Acharya's words, Shachi-devi [said]:
"He is still just a boy; let Him study now.

**724.** My son has lost His father — let Him study for some more days;
see to that instead, so that He may become accomplished."

**725.** Hearing this, the Acharya was not pleased;
with a downcast face he set out for home.

**726.** He goes weeping and weeping, distressed at heart,
calling aloud: "O, O Gorachand!

**727.** You did not do it for my lot, O Purifier of the fallen —
why then do You bear the name 'Wish-fulfilling Tree'?

**728.** If You Yourself have not fulfilled my desire,
how will You bear the name 'Wish-fulfilling Tree'?

**729.** Glory, glory to You who took away Draupadi's shame and fear!
Glory to You who saved the king of elephants from the crocodile's jaws!

**730.** Glory to You who gave deliverance to Ajamila and the courtesan![^17-6]
Deliver me too, Father of all the world!"

**731.** Meanwhile, at His teacher's house, the Lord knew in His heart
how stricken with grief the Acharya was.

**732.** Hastily gathering His books, Bhagavan,
taking leave of His teacher, the Lord set out.

**733.** His gait was beautiful, like that of an intoxicated elephant;
His golden body sparkled with ornaments.

**734.** His curling locks enchant the whole world;
His lips are bandhuli flowers, His teeth pearls.

**735.** His limbs, anointed with sandal, are beautiful;
on His body fine garments that captivate the heart.

**736.** Gaurahari is the king of many millions of Kamadevas,
and His body throws chaste wives into turmoil.[^17-7]

**737.** The Lord hastened for the Acharya's sake —
that is why He is called the "Wish-fulfilling Tree".

**738.** And the Acharya goes weeping from road to road
and runs with arms raised, crying: "O, O Gorachand!"

**739.** Just then Mahaprabhu, coming back from His teacher's house,
met the Acharya.

**740.** The Acharya fell at His feet, stretched out like a stick,
and Mahaprabhu, smiling and smiling, raised him up.

**741.** Greeting him, He embraced him tightly
and asked sweetly: "Where have you been?"

**742.** The Acharya says: "Listen, listen, Vishvambhara:
I went to Your house.

**743.** To Your mother, the goddess, so well-mannered,
I made known what was in my heart:

**744.** there is a girl worthy to be Your bride —
the daughter of Vallabha Acharya, renowned for every virtue.

**745.** Your mother paid no heed to these words,
and I am going home with a heavy heart."

**746.** Hearing this, the Lord said nothing,
but with a playful smile went home.

**747.** Seeing that playfulness, that beauty, that soft, sweet smile,
the Acharya was filled with hope:

**748.** "My business will surely succeed," he understood;
he understood in his heart that the Lord would marry.

**749.** The Acharya came home rejoicing,
pondering in his heart all that the Lord had done.

**750.** Coming home, Vishvambhara said to His mother:
"What answer did you give Vanamali Acharya?

**751.** I saw him going along the road downcast;
talking with the Acharya gave Me no joy.

**752.** Why have you displeased him?
Seeing him downcast, I was saddened."

**753.** Hearing her son's words, the very shrewd Shachi
understood the hint and made haste.

**754.** At once a man went to fetch the Acharya;
hearing the news, he came quickly.

**755.** His whole body filled with joy, his voice choked with emotion,
he came before Shachi and bowed.

**756.** Prostrating himself, he took the dust of her feet:
"Why have you called me, mistress?"

**756+1.** Hearing the Acharya's words, Shachi-devi
then spoke to him with respect:[^17-8]

**757.** "What you spoke of before — arrange it;
I shall marry Vishvambhara, to everyone's joy.

**758.** You love my Vishvambhara more than I do;
you will do everything yourself — what should I tell you?

**759.** What you spoke of for Vishvambhara's marriage —
undertake it yourself, I tell you."

**760.** Hearing this, Vanamali, the excellent Acharya,
said: "I shall carry out your command."

**761.** Saying this, he went to the house of Vallabha Acharya;
Vallabha Acharya rose to meet him with great respect,

**762.** humbly offered him a seat
and, counting it his good fortune, said with a smile:

**763.** "By my good fortune you have come to me;
and if there is some other business — tell me now."

**764.** Hearing the words of Vallabha Mishra, the Acharya
set out at length the business of his heart:

**765.** "You are always kind to me;
out of affection for you I have come to your house.

**766.** The son of Mishra Purandara, Sri Vishvambhara, —
in lineage, character and virtues He is beautiful in every way.

**767.** Can I describe His virtues?
The Creator has gathered all virtues together in one.

**768.** What shall I say of His virtues — all people sing them;
you will hear of them from the mouths of all.

**769.** Vishvambhara is a bridegroom worthy of your daughter;
I have said everything — if it pleases your heart."

**770.** Hearing this, Mishra reflected and said:
"By my good fortune you have spoken these words to me.

**771.** I am poor, I cannot give anything [as dowry];
I have only one daughter — a beauty.

**772.** Knowing this, if you yourself so direct,
I shall give my daughter to Vishvambhara, a jewel of a son-in-law.

**773.** I shall gladden the gods, the rishis and the ancestors
when I give my daughter in marriage to Gaurachandra.

**774.** Such a thing comes only as the fruit of much austerity;
there is no greater friend than you — I have told you my heart.

**775.** This has been my heart's wish day and night,
but I did not dare to say it aloud."

**776.** Thus the two came to an agreement,
and the Acharya reported everything to Shachi.

**777.** Hearing it, Shachi-devi was very pleased
and blessed Vanamali Acharya.

**778.** Calling her friends and kinsfolk, she made it known to them;
her whole body filled with joy, she rejoiced.

**779.** All the kinsfolk and brothers gave their consent;
having considered it, all said: "Good, good."

*Here ends the Pauganda-lila.*

[^17-1]: Sarasvati is the goddess of learning and speech; the Lord is the Master of all knowledge (cf. Murari 1.08.12: "husband of Sarasvati").
[^17-2]: Literally "maya-manusha-vigraha", "a human form by maya": said of the Lord, maya is His yogamaya; so also in Murari (1.09.3).
[^17-3]: Literally "the speech of the bangajas" — natives of East Bengal (cf. Murari 1.09.4). The Lord's forefathers came from Shrihatta, and He loved to mimic the East Bengali accent in jest.
[^17-4]: Vallabhacharya's daughter is Lakshmipriya, Lakshmi herself, who appeared in order to become the Lord's consort; Bhaktisiddhanta in his summary (en/18) calls her Lakshmi-devi. Cf. Murari 1.09.7.
[^17-5]: That is, she bowed to His feet in her mind. The 1983 edition reads: "took upon her head the dust of the Lord's lotus feet".
[^17-6]: Draupadi, wife of the Pandavas, whom Krishna saved from disgrace in the assembly of the Kauravas; the king of elephants is Gajendra (Bhagavata, Canto 8); Ajamila (Bhagavata, Canto 6) was saved by uttering the name of Narayana; the courtesan is probably Pingala (Bhagavata 11.8).
[^17-7]: Literally "a body that spreads disgrace among chaste wives", that is, so beautiful that they lose their composure: a motif of Lochana Dasa's nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9).
[^17-8]: V. 756+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition does not have it.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 10 (portion 18)

*The beginning of the Kaishora-lila: preparations for Vishvambhara's wedding; the adhivasa of bridegroom and bride; the women of Nadia go to fetch water for the rite; the wedding morning — the nandimukha-shraddha, the tears of Shachi and of the Lord for His departed father; the bridegroom's attire and the wedding procession, the rejoicing of Nadia*

## Kaishora-lila (The Pastimes of Youth): the Wedding

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One day, as Gaurasundara, having finished His lessons, was returning home from His teacher's house, He met Vanamali Acharya on the way. Talking with the Acharya, Gaurahari understood that he had gone to His mother Shachi-devi to arrange His marriage, but, not having received the hoped-for answer from Shachi-devi, was returning home with a saddened heart. Without saying a word to the Acharya on the way, Gaurahari went home and by a hint let Mother Shachi know His wish; then Mother Shachi again summoned the matchmaker Vanamali Acharya and told him to arrange her son's marriage. Receiving Mother Shachi's command, Vanamali Acharya came to the house of Vallabhacharya and settled the marriage of Gaurahari with his daughter Lakshmi-devi. When the marriage had been agreed upon, Shachi-devi called her relatives and neighbours and announced her son's wedding, and all were immersed in an ocean of supreme bliss. Meanwhile Shachi-devi set about making all kinds of preparations in the house for her son's wedding. Before long the day of the adhivasa arrived. The respectable ladies, following ancient popular custom, duly performed the anointing of the body with turmeric, the fetching of water[^18-1] and other rites; the Vedic rites too were performed as prescribed. In Vallabhacharya's house all these rites were likewise duly performed. Then on the wedding day Gaurahari, with great pomp, in a festive procession and with many companions, arrived at the house of Vallabhacharya, and the Acharya, according to ancient custom, received his son-in-law by offering Him water for His feet and arghya. Then the Acharya brought his daughter Lakshmi-devi to the wedding pavilion and gave her to Gaurahari. And after completing the rites of kushandika,[^18-2] the feeding of brahmanas and so on, he sent his daughter to his son-in-law's house.

*Raga varadi; disha: "Dvijachand is my life!"*

**1.** Then Shachi, looking at her son's face,
says to Him sweet words with a smile:

**2.** "Listen, listen, Vishvambhara, my golden son:
the daughter of Vallabha Acharya is simply wonderful.

**3.** I feel she is worthy to be Your wife;
what good fortune to have such a daughter-in-law!

**4.** Consider it and fix a splendid time;
gather all that is proper."

**5.** Hearing His mother's words, Vishvambhara Raya
got ready all that was needed.

**6.** He called an astrologer and an excellent pandit,
and they fixed the auspicious moment and time.

**7.** That auspicious day, that auspicious time arrived;
brahmanas and all good people came joyfully.

**8.** The whole town of Nadia was filled with joy;
the ocean of prema surged, and all forgot themselves.

**9.** Gathering the married women,[^18-3] Shachi performs the auspicious rites;
all the acharyas perform the Lord's adhivasa.

**10.** On all sides the brahmanas recite the Vedas;
conchs and mridangas sound — auspicious signs.

**11.** All the quarters are adorned with rows of lamps and flags;
fragrant sandal, garlands — everything is beautiful.

**12.** All the brahmanas performed the Lord's adhivasa,
and He revealed a body as beautiful as millions of Kamadevas.

**13.** His body sparkles, all is lit by its radiance;
seeing it, all the brahmanas were amazed.

**14.** The brahmanas were given fragrant sandal and garlands
