and, with gift after gift of betel, were greatly pleased.

**15.** Vallabha Acharya performs the bride's adhivasa;
with the best of brahmanas he performed the auspicious rites.

**16.** Exchanging fragrant garlands, sandal and scents,
at the adhivasa they adorned the jewel of a son-in-law.

**17.** When the adhivasa was finished towards the end of the night,
[the women] came rejoicing, saying: "Let us go for the water!"[^18-4]

**18.** All at once, in waves, all kinds of instruments sounded,
and all the respectable wives forgot their vows.

**19.** The young women in the town of Nadia lost their senses
in the waves of the ocean of rasa of Gauranga's wedding.[^18-5]

**20.** In throngs go the nagaris — wives of brahmanas —
like moons adorning the circle of the earth.

**21.** Eyes like a doe's, gait like a she-elephant's,
the radiance of their bodies sparkles, humbling Madana's pride.

**22.** Their hair, dress, garments and ornaments are matchless:
one glance at them would steal even a sage's heart.

**23.** In their smiles lightning trembles, in their speech is nectar;
laughing and jesting, they go swaying along.

**24.** They sing of Gauranga's virtues in sweet melodies;
at the sound of the fifth note Ananga's body trembles.

**25.** In their noses pearl-rings sway —
as if stars were falling onto the red disc [of dawn].

**26.** The respectable wives came to Shachi's house;
all were given scents, betel nuts and sandal.

**27.** All the nagaris went for the water;
in every house there is auspicious joy.

*Sung in raga tudi*

**28.** A moonlit night, moon-faced maidens;
with sweet voices, friends, we shall sing of Gora's lilas.

*Refrain: "Who, who will go in front, friends, who will sing of Gora's virtues, friends? Let us go for the water! The heart surges — who can hold it back?"*

**29.** One in silken dress, another in yellow garments;
swaying, they go [fanned by] the breeze from Gora's body.

**30.** "Let Shachi go in front, and we shall go behind her;
coming and going, friends, we shall stand near Gora.

**31.** Take fragrant sandal and garlands in your hands, covered:
on that pretext I shall touch Gora's body.

**32.** Take camphor and betel carefully in your hands;
hand in hand, I shall place them right into Gora's hand."

**33.** The married women, gathered together in play and merriment,
brought the water — so sings this Lochana Dasa of [the Lord's] virtues.

*Raga bhatiyari*

**34.** In joy and gladness the night passed; at the bright dawn
with joyful hearts they performed the rites according to rule.

**35.** They performed the bathing and the giving of gifts, as was proper;
as prescribed, they worshipped the gods and the ancestors.

**36.** They performed the nandimukha-shraddha as the Vedas ordain;
abundant food was given to the brahmanas.

**37.** To dancers and to the bards gifts were given;
with gifts of every kind all were pleased.

**38.** But they valued [the Lord's] sweet words more than the gifts:
seeing His moonlike face, the heart is refreshed.

**39.** He satisfied each one as he had hoped;
then the Lord bathes again, as befits the wedding.

**40.** Then the barber did his work,
and the respectable wives together anoint His divine body.

**40+1.** Gora is an ocean of beauty woven of moonlit nectar;
the hearts of the young women sank in it — they do not know how to swim.[^18-6]

**40+2.** At touching Him the bodies of all grew faint;
their speech falters, streams of tears flow from their eyes.

**40+3.** On beholding the Lord's face — what feeling rose in them! —
burning deep in their hearts with the fever of Madana, they sank down powerless.

**40+4.** One, holding His arm, stands all atremble;
another stays still, rubbing His divine body with fragrant paste.

**40+5.** One in bliss pressed His two feet to her breast
and bound them fast with the creepers of her arms.

**40+6.** One, as if painted in a picture, gazes fixedly at Gauranga;
another, in the waves of Madana, pours water on His head.

**40+7.** One, as if mad, laughs again and again:
seeing Gauranga's face, they forgot even the duty of a faithful wife.

**40+8.** They bathed the Lord with the water of the celestial river;
seeing this, all the people rocked on waves of bliss.

**40+9.** When, His bath completed, He sat down on His seat,
the nagaris surrounded the son of Shachi.

**41.** All kinds of instruments sound with sweet tones;
on all sides the cries of "ulu-ulu" and "Jaya, jaya!" are heard.

**42.** Then Shachi-devi, taking with her all the married women,
lovingly honours each as befits her.

**43.** She honoured all the kinsfolk who had come to the house
and spoke to all, opening her heart:

**44.** "I, wretched one, have lost my husband, my son is fatherless;
how can I, poor as I am, honour you?"[^18-7]

**45.** As she said this, Shachi's voice faltered;
tears from her eyes soaked the garment over her breast.

**46.** When Shachi spoke these grieving words,
the Lord Vishvambhara, hearing them, bowed His head.

**47.** He began to think: "Where has My father gone?"
His heart began to burn; He felt great pain.

**48.** Tears fall from His eyes like a string of pearls;
seeing this, the goddess Shachi-rani was alarmed.

**49.** And all the respectable wives who were beside her,
seeing the Lord's tears, began to weep.

**50.** "Why, why, child, do I see Your face so sad?
At such an auspicious time — and You weep?

**51.** In all the world You are my only wealth;
if You are dejected, I shall give up my life this very moment."

**52.** Hearing His mother's words, the Lord Vishvambhara,
grieving for His father, speaks in a faltering voice.

**53.** His face was pale, like the moon at dawn;
[His voice] like the deep thunder of a fresh cloud.

**54.** The Lord said to His mother: "Listen to what I say:
why is there such pain in your heart?

**55.** What wealth do you lack? What sorrow has befallen you?
You speak so bitterly, as if you were poor and alone.

**56.** You have reminded Me that I no longer see My father —
and how My heart feels, how can I tell?

**57.** Give each person betel and sandal twice over,
give all kinds of gifts — as much as your heart desires.

**58.** Anoint everyone's whole body with fragrant sandal;
give abundantly, have no worry in your mind.

**59.** What no one on earth does for people,
I shall accomplish by a mere sign — I tell you."[^18-8]

**60.** Hearing these words, Shachi spoke very softly
and with sweet words calmed Vishvambhara.

**61.** Just as Vishvambhara had directed,
so she satisfied all the brahmanas.

**62.** Meanwhile Vallabha Acharya in his own house
worships the gods and ancestors together with brahmanas.

**63.** To his daughter he gave all kinds of ornaments
and adorned her with scents, sandal and garlands.

**64.** Seeing that the auspicious moment was near, the best of brahmanas
sent a brahmana to fetch the bridegroom.

**65.** Here, meanwhile, the Lord Vishvambhara, with His friends,
arrays His divine body in wonderful attire.

**66.** His body was anointed with scents and sandal;
on His forehead a tilaka like a moonbeam.

**67.** On His cheeks sparkle makara-shaped earrings;
on His chest shines a pearl necklace.

**68.** His red lotus eyes are brightly lined with kajal;
His two brows are like the two bows of Kama.

**69.** Armlets, bracelets, divine rings set with jewels;
a divine radiance sparkles — one cannot look at it.

**70.** He wears a divine garland and a cloth with a red border;
the breeze from His body is laden with fragrance.

**71.** In His hand a golden mirror, like the full moon;
seeing Him, people are no longer masters of their hearts.

**72.** The young wives lost their composure seeing His beauty;
seeing His beauty, the women cannot take their eyes away.

**72+1.** The women are restless, their garments loosened:
He crushed [their hearts] as the king of birds crushes the race of serpents.[^18-9]

**72+2.** At one and the same moment He stole the hearts of all,
caught the fish of their minds and holds it in the net of His beauty.

**72+3.** The doe-eyed women, gazing at Gauranga,
cannot utter a word, nor hold back their hearts.

**72+4.** Drawn by the play of His brows, the playful women
feel their hearts swaying every moment.

**72+5.** She into whose heart the sweetness of His smile has entered
swoons deep within from the pain of Madana.

**72+6.** Longing to touch the rasa of the play of His arms,
the pride of the proud beauties slinks away into hiding.

**73.** Bowing to His mother, the Lord sets out at the auspicious moment;
auspicious cries arose — "Jaya!" and the name of Hari.

**74.** The Lord mounts a divine palanquin, surrounded by His friends;
in front dancers dance and singers sing songs.[^18-10]

**75.** Brahmanas recite the Vedas, bards their panegyrics;
shinga and bargo horns sound, bheur and kahal horns.

**76.** Damama and dagar kettledrums sound, pataha drums and mridangas;
dosari and mohari pipes play — a joy to hear.[^18-11]

**77.** Hearing "Hari, Haribol!" and the cries of "Jaya, jaya!",
the people of Nadia went mad with joy.

**78.** People run, jostling one another, unable to find a way;
a thrill ran through the assembly of townsfolk.

**79.** Some do not tie up their hair, do not adjust their clothes —
they run headlong to see, breathing hard.

**80.** Whispering, exchanging glances, with no more shame,
calling to one another, all the people of Nadia run.

**81.** The proud ones, casting all their pride far away,
run joyfully to see Gauranga.

**81+1.** The playful women heed neither road nor roadless ground:
on the waves of Ananga all the women run.[^18-12]

**82.** In the sky the gods on divine chariots look down
and with love press forward to see Gora's body.

**83.** The wives of the gods gaze at Vishvambhara's face;
on all sides celestial women sing auspicious songs.

*Raga bihagara; refrain: "On all sides cries of 'Jaya, jaya!' are heard — the wedding of Gauranga-chand! The respectable wives together raise the 'ulu-ulu': sing auspicious songs with joy!"*

**84.** "Do up your hair, put on silk saris,
put kajal on your eyes!"
To the wedding of Sri Vishvambhara, all gathering together,
dressed up, they set out.

**85.** "Necklaces, armlets, bracelets, belts with little bells,
anklets — put them on quickly!"
Sindura on the forehead close to the locks,
and below it a dot of sandal.

**86.** Betel on their lips, betel in the left hand,
they go playfully swaying;
seeing Vishvambhara, like the Five-arrowed One,[^18-13]
they cannot keep their composure.

**86+1.** Chewing betel, smiling,
their teeth open like buds of kunda jasmine;
by lips red as bandhuli flowers the bee-like teeth
sit close, greedy for honey.[^18-14]

**86+2.** In rows upon rows the nagaris
went eagerly with the graceful gait of swans;
all the rasa of Madana spreads through their hearts,
their wide eyes stay fixed.

**87.** All kinds of instruments sound, hundreds of conchs resound,
mridangas, pataha drums and kahal horns;
joyfully the dundubhis sound and the dindima drums,
sweetly the muhari pipes play.

**88.** The vinas play, the flutes softly speak,
rababs, upangas, pakhoyajs;
in the town of Nadia there is joy in every house,
auspicious wedding music plays.

**89.** All the people gaze at Gaurachandra's face,
all the people of Nadia rejoice;
praising that beauty which surpasses millions of Kamadevas,
they gaze at Him, forgetting all shame.

**90.** Braids come loose, garments go unadjusted —
they run in a frenzy;
forgetting husbands and sons, faces uncovered,
their hair falling over their breasts.

**91.** "Blessed, blessed, blessed!" say the women,
no other word is heard;
on all sides, in markets and roads, crowds of nagaris
rose up to see.

**92.** Some play the vina, some sing songs,
some run in delight;
on all sides "Jaya, jaya!", the auspicious triumph —
so says Lochana Dasa.

[^18-1]: The fetching of water (jal-saha; in Lochana Dasa "pani sahi"): on the morning of the wedding the married women go singing to the Ganga to fetch water for the bridegroom's bath (below, vv. 17–33).
[^18-2]: Kushandika is the rite at the sacred fire that concludes the wedding (in Lochana Dasa, on the day after the wedding).
[^18-3]: "Aiho-suho" are married women whose husbands are living: only they take part in the auspicious wedding rites.
[^18-4]: "Pani sahi" — see note 1.
[^18-5]: Here and below (vv. 18–33, 72, 78–81, 84–92) is a favourite motif of Lochana Dasa: the women of Nadia, forgetting shame, are drawn to Gaura (nagari-bhava; see en/09, note 9). Still more such lines are in the 1983 edition (vv. 40+1 – 40+9, 72+1 – 72+6, 81+1, 86+1 – 86+2); they are absent in A.
[^18-6]: Vv. 40+1 – 40+9 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them in brackets (except v. 40+8) — the way that editor marks lines found in only some manuscripts. This is Lochana Dasa's motif of nagari-bhava (see note 5 and en/09, note 9). "Madana" (Kamadeva) here is an image of the hearts' attraction to Gaura; for a Gaudiya Vaishnava the Lord is the transcendental (aprakrita) Madana, and such attraction to Him, as in the gopis, is not worldly passion; Bhaktisiddhanta, however, does not accept worship of Gaura in the mood of "Gaura-nagari". "Forgot even the duty of a faithful wife" (v. 40+7) — like the gopis, who left family dharma for Krishna's sake.
[^18-7]: By custom a widow takes no part in the auspicious wedding rites and cannot honour the guests as before; hence Shachi's grief. Cf. Murari 1.10.5–12.
[^18-8]: That is, the Lord, encouraging His mother, promises to do for the guests by His mere sign more than people can; cf. Murari (1.10.11): "I have the power to do what is beyond mortals, and yet I follow the customs of the world."
[^18-9]: Vv. 72+1 – 72+6 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them in brackets (together with v. 72). The king of birds is Garuda, the enemy of serpents.
[^18-10]: In the 1983 edition there are two couplets instead of this one (a different redaction): "The Lord mounts a divine palanquin, surrounded by His friends; seeing this, all the people rejoice at heart. The Lord sets out with His friends; in front a dancer dances, singers sing." A and the 1903 edition have one couplet; we follow A.
[^18-11]: Shinga is a horn; bargo, a wind instrument; damama and dagar, large kettledrums; dosari and mohari (muhari), pipes like the shahnai. For bheur, kahal and the others see the glossary.
[^18-12]: V. 81+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has it in brackets (together with v. 81). Ananga ("the Bodiless") is Kamadeva.
[^18-13]: The Five-arrowed One is Kamadeva, the god of love, armed with five flower-arrows.
[^18-14]: Stanzas 86+1 – 86+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them in brackets. Bandhuli (bandhuka) is a bright red flower, a usual simile for lips.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 11 (portion 19)

*Kaishora-lila, the wedding (conclusion): the Lord at Vallabhacharya's house; the welcoming of the bridegroom; the bride is brought to the Lord, their first sight of each other; Vallabha honours his son-in-law and gives Him his daughter; the night in the bridal chamber; the kushandika, Vallabha's parting words; the return home with Lakshmi, Shachi receives the couple; a song of the Lord's strolls with His friends (1983 edition only)*

*Raga bhatiyari; disha: "Behold, my heart, the wonder: the Darling of Navadvipa, dear as life!" (murchha)*

**93.** Thus [the Lord] arrived at Vallabha Acharya's house;
cries of "Jaya, jaya!" filled the sky.

**94.** Hundreds upon hundreds of lamps are burning, the whole earth is lit,
and in their light the beauty of Gora's body shines.

**95.** Then Vallabha Mishra, offering water for the feet and arghya,
brought the bridegroom into the house with auspicious rites.

**96.** Then Mahaprabhu, going up onto the wedding platform,[^19-1]
joyfully stood upon the seat.

**97.** His face surpasses the full moon on the night of the full moon,
and on it is a sweet smile, as if blended with nectar.

**98.** The radiance of His body is like molten gold,
the build of His body like Mount Meru.

**99.** On His arms are armlets and bracelets, rings set with jewels;
His palms sparkle like red lotuses.

**100.** A splendid garland of malati jasmine swings on Gora's body —
like the waves of the Ganga upon the summit of Sumeru.

**101.** Beneath His crown His forehead is beautifully adorned;
millions of Kamas, seeing it, stand abashed and ashamed.

**102.** Earrings swing at His ears — to what can I compare them?
They have taken from proud beauties all desire for pride.

**103.** While Mahaprabhu stood thus on the platform,
the married women came there for the welcoming of the bridegroom.[^19-2]

**104.** They had put on splendid attire and garments;
in their hands were bright lamps, in their hearts jubilation.

**105.** Before and behind went the married women, and with them the bride's mother:
the noble lady herself went to welcome the bridegroom.

**106.** Circling [Him] seven times with seven lamps in her hands,
with a joyful heart she poured curd upon His feet.

**107.** Having welcomed the bridegroom, the lady went into the house;
the auspicious moment came — the hour of dusk.[^19-3]

**108.** Then Vallabha Acharya, the best of brahmanas,
quickly ordered his daughter to be brought.

**109.** The beautiful maiden [sat] on a finely made seat;
the radiance of her body lit up the earth.

**110.** Jewelled lamps are burning all around her;
her face surpassed the splendour of the full moon.

**111.** On all her body were ornaments of jewels and gold;
their radiance drove the darkness away.

**112.** Circling the Lord seven times,
[the bride], joining her palms at her head, bows to Him.

**113.** The cloth that screened them was removed, and they saw each other;
they gaze at each other — and the eyes of both are dancing.

**114.** As if the Moon and Rohini had met together:[^19-4]
the two shower each other with flowers, as if in a duel.

**115.** Like Hara and Parvati — so the two met;
both, beside themselves with bliss, waved the chhamuni.[^19-5]

**116.** On all sides are cries of "Jaya!" and shouts of "Hari, Hari!";
all the people dance, mad with joy.

**117.** Then the Lord of Kamala, Lord Vishvambhara,
sat down, seating the bride at His left.

**118.** Her face bowed in shyness, she sat beside the Lord;
and Mishra honours his son-in-law according to all the rules.

**119.** He to whose lotus feet Brahma offered water
and by whose grace became the creator of the world;[^19-6]

**120.** He from whose foot the Ganga came down to the earth,[^19-7]
and at that time all the worlds attained liberation;

**121.** He to whom Bali gave three steps of land
and on whose head He placed the dust of His lotus feet;

**122.** That foot by repeating [the name of] which the yogi became Maheshvara,
that foot which the goddess Kamala serves with joy;

**123.** He from whom comes Vishnu, His partial avatara;
whose portion is Adi-Varaha, who raised up the earth;

**124.** whose portions are Matsya, Kurma, Varaha, Nrisimha and the others,
[the Slayer of] Hiranyakashipu, Vamana, [the Chastiser of] the mlecchas and the rest;

**125.** Parashurama, Bhrigurama, Buddha, the sage Vyasa,
from whose lips the eighteen Puranas are heard[^19-8] —

**126.** hear now this song of the virtues of the ten avataras,
who descend age after age to deliver the souls —

**127.** that Lord became Vallabhacharya's son-in-law;
in all the three worlds there are no words for his good fortune.

**128.** The song of Gauranga's virtues is a morsel of nectar:
whoever hears it, from his heart godlessness departs.

**129.** To such lotus feet Mishra offers water —
feet whose worship dispels the darkness of samsara.

**130.** To Him to whom great Indra yielded his royal throne,[^19-9]
Mishra gives a seat — a mat of grass.

**131.** To the Lord who is clad in divine yellow garments
he gives a garment — it is fearful even to hear!

**132.** Thus, in due order, all that was prescribed —
the sacrifice and all the other rites — was completed.

**133.** No one is as fortunate as Vallabha Acharya:
the Lord of Vaikuntha Himself accepted his daughter as a gift.

**134.** What can I say of the boundless good fortune of Vallabha Mishra,
in whose house the Lord performed the rite of the five mouthfuls![^19-10]

**135.** Bride and bridegroom took their meal in one room;
hundreds upon hundreds of wives of noble families gathered in the bridal chamber.[^19-11]

**136.** In throngs the young women came to the Lord
and surrounded Vishvambhara in front and behind.

**137.** Seeing the rising of the smile on His moonlike face,
the darkness of everyone's shyness fled away.

**137+1.** All the beauties sat down near the Lord;[^19-12]
at the breeze from His body the bodies of the playful women tremble.

**137+2.** The garments and the speech of all began to slip,
and the eyes of some grew heavy with languor.

**137+3.** One, touching His body, in the waves of Ananga's play
sank down powerless upon Vishvambhara.

**137+4.** Another, unblinking, gazes at Him with a fixed look —
as the chakora is blissful gazing at the moon.

**137+5.** With the lotuses of their eyes all worship Gora's face;
one begs for His touch upon herself.

**137+6.** Like a dependent pauper who has found great wealth —
there is nowhere to hide it, yet out of attachment he cannot let it go.

**138.** In the bridal chamber some start the game of changing names;
enraptured by Vishvambhara's virtues, they jest.

**139.** One says: "Vishvambhara, listen to me:
give a betel nut — Lakshmi is overcome with sleep.

**140.** Put it into Lakshmi's mouth Yourself —
let all her friends watch with delight!"

**140+1.** One loosens Vishvambhara's hair and binds it again —
her eagerness to bind it comes from her longing to touch [Him].[^19-13]

**140+2.** Another puts a betel nut into Gorachand's mouth —
her heart trembles, she feels great bliss.

**140+3.** Another, swaying faint, her heart in a tumult,
lifts Lakshmi and places her on Gorachand's lap.

**141.** Another says: "Is anyone more fortunate than she?
She has gained a husband like Vishvambhara!

**142.** What austerity did she perform, what vows, what gifts?
What meditation did she perfect in the worship of the gods?

**143.** What faithful, chaste wife on earth
could keep her heart steady on seeing Vishvambhara's beauty?

**144.** His beautiful face surpasses Madana's abode;
He is the thief of the finest jewel — a proud beauty's heart.

**145.** His mighty arms surpass the staff of Kama:
women long to clasp them to their breasts.

**146.** Lakshmi will delight in all this body —
but when shall we be blessed with His touch?

**147.** This is our hope: to become His maidservants;
when shall we serve Sri Gauranga, who is like the moon?"[^19-14]

*Raga varadi; disha: "My life, O Gorachand, are haya!"*

**148.** Thus, in amusement and merriment, morning came;
the Lord performed the morning rites as prescribed.

**149.** On the day after the wedding came the rite of kushandika;
brahmanas are fed, as befits brahmanas.

**150.** The Lord performed all this there that day
and said: "Tomorrow I shall go home."

**151.** The Lord set out for home with a joyful heart;
the family honour Him with silver and gold.

**152.** The Lord sits on one seat with Lakshmi at His left;
all around, the women surrounded them.

**153.** In Vallabha Mishra's heart are joy and sorrow;
at the departure he blesses bride and bridegroom.

**154.** Offering durva grass, rice, scents, garlands, betel nuts and sandal,
he speaks to his son-in-law these words:

**155.** "I am poor and wretched, I have no good fortune;
what gift can I give You — what is worthy of You?

**156.** Only by Your own goodness have You shown me grace:
by accepting my daughter You have made me blessed.

**157.** What can I say to You, Lord — what worth have I?
Only by Your own goodness have You become my son-in-law.

**158.** Having found refuge at Your lotus feet, which grant fearlessness,
I shall no longer suffer at the hands of Yama.

**159.** The gods and the ancestors are pleased with me
since I gave You my daughter.

**160.** The feet which Brahma, Shiva and others worship in meditation —
those feet I have worshipped in person, as prescribed.

**161.** I shall say something more — listen, Vishvambhara."
As he said this, his voice faltered.

**162.** His eyes brimmed with tears of tenderness;
taking Lakshmi's hand, he placed it in Vishvambhara's hand.

**163.** "From this day I entrust Lakshmi to You;
knowing [all this], care for her and protect her.

**164.** In my house Lakshmi was the mistress of the house;
from this day she is Your maidservant, the daughter-in-law of Your family.

**165.** In my house she lived as she pleased:
she would be stubborn until her mother fed her [herself].

**166.** In my house she grew up in her mother's and father's arms;
wherever we came back from, she would throw her arms round our necks.

**167.** Lakshmi is everyone's darling; I have no son —
in all my house I have only this one little girl.

**168.** But what am I saying — she is Your own.
All that I say I say blinded by attachment.

**169.** All this I have said out of my foolishness:
what does my attachment matter, when You are her husband?[^19-15]

**170.** In the three worlds no one is as fortunate as Lakshmi;
all that I say comes only from love and attachment."

**171.** Having said this, Mishra fell silent;
his reddened eyes were brimming with tears of sorrow.

**172.** And Vishvambhara set out, with His beloved at His left:
together with Lakshmi He mounted the palanquin.[^19-16]

**173.** Conchs and dundubhis sound, cries of "Jaya, jaya!" resound;
all kinds of instruments play, joy spreads in waves.

**174.** Brahmanas recite the Vedas, bhat bards sing songs of praise;
in front the dancers dance — the joy is boundless.

**175.** Surrounded by His friends, the Lord travels along the road;
in the sky the gods follow in divine chariots.

**176.** Meanwhile Shachi, joyful, gathering the married women,
bustles about merrily at her son's festival.

**177.** At the door she set an auspicious pitcher with sprigs of leaves
and placed a coconut on top of it.

**178.** All is ready for the nirmanchhana, lamps of ghee are burning;
at that auspicious hour the Lord arrived home.

**179.** The women perform Vishvambhara's nirmanchhana;[^19-17]
cries of "Jaya, jaya!", "ulu-ulu", fine songs and dancing.

**180.** All kinds of instruments play, the joy is boundless;
Shachi's house was filled with every happiness.

**181.** Auspicious cries arose, the joy was great;
taking Lakshmi by the hand, [the Lord] entered His home.

**182.** Shachi-devi embraces her son and daughter-in-law;
showering them with durva grass and rice, she says: "Live long!"

**183.** She kisses her son's face, looking at her daughter-in-law,
and kisses her daughter-in-law's face, gazing at her son.

**184.** Shachi's dwelling was filled with every happiness;
joyfully Lochana Dasa sings of Gora's virtues.[^19-18]

*Raga sindhura*

**184+1.** Thus the Lord lives happily
with His friends and kinsmen;
in Shachi's heart is an ocean of bliss
as she sees Gorachand with His bride.[^19-19]

**184+2.** Gora, the delight of Nadia,
absorbed in the joy of play;
His brows are Kama's bow,
the arrows are the glances of His eyes.

**184+3.** Among His friends, in jests and sport,
His body full of the rasa of lila;
on earth, without a cloud, a steady lightning:
[Kama] has thrown down his flower-bow.

**184+4.** Resting His hand on a friend's shoulder,
a book in His left hand,
at the close of day [He goes]
to the bank of the celestial river.

**184+5.** His body anointed with fragrant sandal,
on His brow a most charming tilaka;
at its fragrance the bees are bewildered,
and the young women came running in crowds.

**184+6.** His curling hair, the sweetness of His attire —
who, seeing it, can hold back her heart?
Allured by the beauty of His dress, the young women
do not heed the fear of their elders.

**184+7.** In the town of Nadia, where He is the foremost of nagaras,
all are sunk in an ocean of rasa;
seeing Gaurachandra's lila, all lost their senses,
and their pride was ground to dust.

**184+8.** The nagaris praise His qualities
with sidelong glances of curving eyes;
setting fire to the temple of shame,
they fall [to Him] by hundreds of thousands in longing.

**184+9.** The beauties of Nadia, forgetting themselves,
remain absorbed in meditation [on Him] in their hearts.
Lochana Dasa says: in those waves of happiness —
just imagine this.

[^19-1]: "Chhorla" — the raised wedding platform (under a canopy) where the bridegroom stands and the rite is performed.
[^19-2]: "Bar-urati" (in Lochana Dasa, "bara urathite") — the rite of welcoming the bridegroom: the married women, led by the bride's mother, circle him with lamps and bless him.
[^19-3]: Godhuli — "the hour of cow-dust", dusk, when the herds come home; in Bengal an auspicious time for a wedding.
[^19-4]: Rohini — the beloved consort of the Moon god; cf. Murari 1.10.22.
[^19-5]: The meaning of "chhamuni" is unclear (perhaps the covering that hides the faces of bride and groom until the rite of seeing each other); the 1983 edition reads "they left the chhamuni".
[^19-6]: When the Lord as Vamana-Trivikrama strode up to Brahma's abode, Brahma washed His foot; cf. Murari 1.10.24.
[^19-7]: Vv. 120–128 are in A but absent from the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them in a note as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^19-8]: Lochana Dasa's list of avataras is free: "Hiranyakashipu" apparently stands for the avatara who slew him (Nrisimha), "the mlecchas" for Kalki, who chastises them; Bhrigurama is another name of Parashurama (perhaps Rama of the Raghus was meant); Vyasa is a shaktyavesha-avatara. The sense is clear: all the avataras are portions of Him who appeared as Gauranga; so too the Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition teaches (Gaura is Krishna Himself, the source of all avataras).
[^19-9]: Indra, king of the gods, honours the Lord as his master; cf. Murari 1.10.25.
[^19-10]: "Pancha-grasi" — the rite of the five mouthfuls: in the bride's house the bridegroom eats five handfuls of food.
[^19-11]: Basara — the room where the newly-weds spend the wedding night while the women keep them awake with jests and songs.
[^19-12]: Vv. 137+1 – 137+6 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them, partly in brackets. On the nagari-bhava see en/09, note 9; cf. en/18, note 6: Lochana Dasa depicts the attraction of the women of Nadia to Gaura in the language of love poetry, but for a Gaudiya Vaishnava this is not worldly passion but attraction to the Lord, the transcendental Madana. The chakora is a bird which, by poetic tradition, feeds on moonbeams.
[^19-13]: Vv. 140+1 – 140+3 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them.
[^19-14]: Vv. 136–147 — again Lochana Dasa's motif of nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9). Still more such lines are in the 1983 edition (vv. 137+1 – 137+6, 140+1 – 140+3).
[^19-15]: Here "maya" is Vallabha's own fatherly attachment: before such a husband as the Lord it counts for nothing.
[^19-16]: Lit. "a vehicle carried by men".
[^19-17]: Nirmanchhana — a rite in which women wave lamps and auspicious articles around the bridegroom to ward off all that is inauspicious.
[^19-18]: Cf. Murari 1.10.27, 1.11.1–2.
[^19-19]: The song in raga sindhura (vv. 184+1 – 184+9) is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition, where the new section begins at once); the 1903 edition has it. The 1983 edition marks stanza 184+2 as the refrain (dhruva). Vv. 184+4 and 184+6 are partly corrupt in both editions and are translated by sense. "The foremost of nagaras" (184+7) and the stanzas on the nagaris are Lochana Dasa's motif of nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9).

---

# Adi-khanda, part 12 (portion 20)

*Banga-vijaya: the Ganga, loving the Lord, rises to touch His feet; a brahmana devoted to the Ganga recognizes Him as Bhagavan and tells how the Ganga was born from the Lord, who melted at Shiva's singing; the Lord journeys to East Bengal, sanctifies the Padmavati and gives the name of Hari to all; Lakshmi serves Shachi in Navadvipa*

## Kaishora-lila (The Pastimes of Youth): Banga-vijaya — the Lord in East Bengal

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One day, when Sri Sri Mahaprabhu went at sunset to see the Ganga, Ganga-devi, seeing her beloved Lord, surged up in love and tenderly began to touch His feet again and again. The acharyas, mishras, bhattas and other brahmana pandits who at that time, having finished their evening prayers on the bank of the Ganga, were praising the Ganga, were astonished to see the water suddenly rise and fell to pondering; and then a certain brahmana devoted to the Ganga, by her grace, came to know that the Lord is Bhagavan Himself. In explaining the cause of this rising of the water the author relates a Puranic story: once the devarshi Narada, together with Mahadeva and Ganesha, was singing the glories of Sri Hari, and at that time Bhagavan Sri Hari Himself appeared there. The perspiration that then came out on His divine body as He heard the kirtana about Himself is the Ganga, Brahman in liquid form. […] Then Bhagavan Gaurahari wished to go to East Bengal […]; having shown boundless mercy to the people of East Bengal, He returned to Navadvipa, but before that Lakshmi-devi, bitten by the serpent of separation from the Lord, disappeared from this world. Therefore Shachi-devi […]; and Gaurahari, consoling His mother, […].[^20-1]

*Raga shri;[^20-2] disha: "How wondrous — behold: the Darling of Navadvipa, dear as life — are haya!"*

**1.** Listen, everyone, to another story:
the song of Vishvambhara's virtues is ever new.

**2.** [He] went with His friends to see the Ganga;
the day was ending, the lovely evening hour had come.

**3.** On both banks of the Ganga all the brahmanas and good people,
bowing to the Ganga, were offering her praises as every day.

**4.** With pitchers on their hips the townswomen go
and, faces unveiled, gaze at the goddess Ganga.

**5.** Countless mishras, acharyas, bhattas and pandits,
so many pious people of excellent conduct —

**6.** all stand on the bank of the Ganga and gaze at her:
the pure waters of the Ganga are adorned with many flowers.

**7.** With scents, sandal, garlands and fine bananas
young men and women, the old and children worship her.

**8.** The Ganga, purifier of the three worlds, flows swiftly:
in her love for the Lord the Ganga cannot contain herself.

**9.** The goddess Ganga surged up, her waters rose,
and with a murmur she touched the Lord's body.

**10.** Longing to touch Him again, the goddess Ganga rises;
the people grew doubtful, thinking to themselves:

**11.** "Every day we see the Ganga — she is always the same,
but today — a wondrous force, a roaring is heard.

**12.** There has been no rain, yet the water rises;
the current runs ever swifter, the waters surge."

**13.** So all the people conjectured.
[But there] was a brahmana devoted to the Ganga.

**14.** By the Ganga's grace his heart was pure,
and that brahmana knew everything — past and future.

**14+1.** He worships the Ganga and chants the name of Hari,
[longing] to see the Ganga and Gauranga in one place.[^20-3]

**14+2.** With this desire in his heart that brahmana
built a hut on the bank of the Ganga and lived there happily.

**15.** Seeing the Ganga's festival, he grows ever more joyful;
he pondered and pondered — and it was revealed to him.

**16.** Suddenly he sees: Mahaprabhu Vishvambhara,
surrounded by His friends, standing by the Ganga.

**17.** The Lord gazes at the Ganga with great love;
His body grew twice its size, so did the hairs on His body stand on end.

**18.** His eyes, reddened with tenderness, brim with tears;
seeing this, the brahmana found a witness [to what had been revealed] to his heart.

**19.** "This is He — Bhagavan, and none other!"
So pondering, he went up to the Lord.

**20.** Coming close to the Lord, he stands and sees:
the Lord is beside Himself with the Ganga's love.

**21.** The Lord in His heart knows the Ganga's heart;
the Ganga comes forward and touches His hands.

**22.** But touching His hands did not fulfil the Ganga's desire:
under the guise of waves the Ganga clings to His feet.

**22+1.** Taking a visible form, the Ganga stands near the Lord;[^20-4]
with joined palms she gazes at His lotus feet.

**22+2.** Seeing this, the brahmana thrilled all over:
"Look, all you people: the Ganga and Gauranga!"

**22+3.** The Ganga touched the Lord's lotus feet
and, her desire fulfilled, went back into her own waters.

**22+4.** No one knew that the Ganga had been near Gauranga;
but the brahmana, attaining his desire, saw it with his own eyes.

**22+5.** Receiving the love of the celestial river, Gaurahari
thrilled all over and trembled.

**23.** In ecstasy the Lord cries "Haribol!"
and, losing control of Himself, embraces His companions.

**24.** He turned crimson — thus prema begins;
the hairs on His body stood up like the filaments of kadamba flowers.

**25.** In her love for the Lord the Ganga abides in His heart:
hundreds of streams flow from the ocean of His eyes.

**26.** Moisture flows from every pore — people say, "It is sweat";
[but this] is the ocean of prema surging — Brahman in liquid form.

**27.** On all sides all the people cry "Hari, Hari!";
the ocean of prema surged in waves of bliss.

**28.** All the people of Nadia were astonished;
the brahmana devoted to the Ganga understood the matter.

**29.** "This Lord, Vishvambharadeva, is Bhagavan Himself:
seeing Him, the Ganga rises" — so he realized.

**30.** Falling at His feet, the brahmana cries aloud:
"At last the Ganga has shown me her grace!

**31.** He whom the best of yogis and sages cannot attain in meditation —
such a Mahaprabhu I have seen today with my own eyes!"

**32.** He rolls on the ground, weeping and crying out;
the brahmana forgot himself in the bliss of prema.

**33.** All the people stand around;
and the brahmana openly tells the ancient story.

**34.** Seeing the brahmana beside himself, Thakura[^20-5] went away
to His home, His heart full of bliss.

**35.** The brahmana tells the original story, and all listen —
how the goddess Ganga was born

**36.** and for what reason the goddess Ganga rises now:
"I shall tell you all — listen attentively.

**37.** Once the great Mahesha Thakura[^20-6]
was singing of Krishna's virtues in great, abundant bliss.

**38.** Narada Thakura sang, and Ganesha played the drum;
the hairs of their bodies stood on end from head to foot.

**39.** All three sang a beautiful melody together;
the waves of sound-Brahman[^20-7] pierced the universe.

**40.** First, it was Mahesha himself, and in the ecstasy of love for Krishna besides;
Narada had his vina, and Ganesha was on the drum as well!

**41.** The Lord, unable to restrain Himself, came to the place
where Mahesha and Narada were singing His virtues together.

**42.** He said: 'Do not sing My virtues, listen, Mahesha:
I do not well understand the essence of your singing.

**43.** At your singing I cannot remain in My body:
the bonds of the body loosen, and it becomes liquid.'

**44.** Hearing Thakura's words, Mahesha smiles:
'I shall sing and see what the essence of this is.'

**45.** So saying, he sings His virtues with still greater delight;
the sound filled the universe — this earth and the sky.

**46.** The Lord's body melted, His form grew thin;[^20-8]
in alarm Mahesha stopped his singing.

**47.** When he stopped singing, [the Lord's] mind grew calm;
this is that water of mercy famed in the world.

**48.** This water of mercy is called Brahman in liquid form;
all proclaim it to be Janardana in the form of a holy place.[^20-9]

**49.** It is very, very rare in this world:
Brahma filled his kamandalu with it and kept it.

**50.** King Bali was a devotee of the Lord;
to show him grace, the Lord appeared [as Vamana].

**51.** The Lord asked for land on which to set three steps:
with two steps He covered the three worlds,

**52.** and the third foot He placed on Bali's head.
There is no other Lord so merciful!

**53.** Hear also another wonder — the glory of the third step,
whose mercy blessed the three worlds:

**54.** with the tip of that foot's toenail the shell of the universe was pierced,
and to that foot Brahma lovingly offered water for washing.[^20-10]

**55.** The water from the Lord's lotus feet [people] reverently place on their heads;
that is why the Ganga is called 'born of the third step'.

**56.** Such is Thakura — Mahaprabhu Vishvambhara:
behold, all of you, here He is before your eyes!

**57.** Seeing Him, the goddess Ganga remembered the past
and in love and tenderness began to rise.

**58.** The Lord looks at the Ganga with a loving gaze,
and Gora's body is sweeter to her than nectar.

**59.** Under the guise of waves she touches His feet again;
I have realized this by inner experience and told you all."

**60.** Hearing this, all the people rejoiced;
joyfully Lochana Dasa sings of Gora's virtues.

*Raga dhanashi; disha: "Are, the sweetness of my Gora's lotus feet! The bees, His devotees, fly up, circle and fall [upon them]. Are are haya!" (murchha). "Listen to this wondrous tale, the song of Gora's virtues — a name auspicious to the ear!"*

**61.** Thus [the Lord] spent some days in happiness,
in joy and play together with His friends.

**62.** One day He suddenly resolved within Himself:
"I shall go to the eastern country — for the good of all people.

**63.** Everyone says it is the land the Pandavas avoided;[^20-11]
[the Padma there] is the Ganga and yet not the Ganga: this is the proof of it.

**64.** By My touch the Padmavati will become blessed;
all the people will know no one but Me."

**65.** Having thus reasoned within Himself,
the Lord told His mother: "I shall go to earn money."

**66.** The Lord sets out on the journey together with His own;
Mother Shachi's heart is tossed with anxiety.

**67.** With an aching heart Shachi says to her son:
"Listen, my child, to what I tell You.

**68.** You are going to a distant land to earn money —
how shall I live without seeing You?

**69.** As a fish without water cannot live,
so it is with me without You.

**70.** Thinking of Your moonlike face,
I shall die, my child, not seeing You."

**71.** Hearing His mother's words, Lord Damodara[^20-12]
humbly answered her, consoling her:

**72.** "Do not fear separation from Me:
I shall soon come back to you."

**73.** To Lakshmi the Lord said with a smile:
"Be diligent in serving My mother."

**74.** Whatever His mother said, the Lord paid no heed:
setting out on the auspicious journey, He goes, softly smiling.

**75.** Mahaprabhu set out with His companions
and travels about, merry, with a joyful heart.

**76.** Wherever Lord Vishvambhara went,
the people there, seeing Him, lost their heads.

**77.** Seeing His beauty, no one can take their eyes away;
some say: "If only we could see this beauty day and night!"

**78.** The women, seeing His face, say:
"Today our birth is fruitful, our eyes are fruitful!

**79.** What fortunate mother bore Him in her womb?
Never have we seen so beautiful a body!

**80.** What fortunate woman, having worshipped Hara and Gauri,
has gained a husband of such beauty, such virtues?

**81.** The radiance of His body surpasses new gold,
the build of His body Mount Sumeru.

**82.** His natural beauty has no equal in the world,
and the sacred thread makes it lovelier still.

**83.** One could die seeing the smile on His beautiful face!"
He entered and remained in the hearts of the loving women.

**84.** One fortunate woman who knew the truth of Krishna's rasa,
guessing, spoke the very essence:[^20-13]

**85.** "Long, beautiful eyes surpassing lotuses,
and in them a wondrous, lovely, tremulous glance.

**86.** It seems to me this is Radha's Beloved in such a form —
I see before me a body of Radha's complexion."[^20-14]

**86+1.** Thus all the young women spoke together;
hearing them, Lord Vishvambhara turned back.[^20-15]

**86+2.** With eyes full of rasa the Lord looked at them all,
and they, overwhelmed with prema, forgot themselves.

**87.** [The Lord] bathed in the Padmavati as prescribed:
by the touch of His feet the river became like the Ganga.

**88.** The swift Padmavati with her sandbanks
is beautiful with crocodiles, tortoises and fish.

**89.** On her banks live brahmanas and good people;
noble men and women bathe at her ghats.

**90.** By Vishvambhara's bath the Padmavati was purified:
she takes away the sins of all who bathe in her.

**91.** Prema-bhakti for Krishna's lotus feet arises
in whoever bathes in her, if only he does not blaspheme the Vaishnavas.

**92.** All who lived on the banks of the Padmavati,
seeing Gaurachandra, made their eyes blessed.

**93.** Gaurahari wanders along the banks of the Padmavati;
that land, clasping His feet, became devoted.

**94.** Receiving His cool feet, the earth became cool;
the goddess thrilled with joy — all that was inauspicious departed.

**95.** Hari first of all delivered that land with great care:
He removed [the stigma] of the land the Pandavas avoided.

**96.** Chandalas and the fallen, the good and the wicked —
the Lord, Himself entreating them, gave all the name of Hari.

**97.** Clean or unclean, what customs, what rules —
He did not regard them and carried all across the ocean of existence.

**98.** Fitting out the boat of the sankirtana of the name,
the Lord, Himself entreating, carried all souls across.

**99.** And whoever runs away — Gaurahari catches and embraces him
and, as the helmsman, carries him to the other shore.

**100.** Never, in any age, has such mercy been heard of:
in what avatara, where, did anyone beg for sins?[^20-16]

**101.** He purified all, equally disposed towards all,
and made them fit for prema for Radha and Krishna.

**102.** The Lord gave knowledge generously, without end:
all became pandits in days, fortnights, months.

**103.** The Lord, the ocean of mercy, master of all the worlds,
revealing His mercy, purified people's minds.

**104.** Thus the Lord stays in the company of good people,
while here in Navadvipa are Lakshmi and Shachi-devi.

**105.** Faithful Lakshmi-devi, for whom her husband is her very life,
serves Shachi joyfully, as is proper.

**106.** She tidies the Deities' room, sweeps and cleans the house,
[prepares] incense, lamps, offerings, scents, garlands and sandal.

**107.** Having prepared everything, she takes it to the Deities' room;
her daughter-in-law's good nature makes Shachi forget herself.

**108.** Shachi-devi is won over by her daughter-in-law's conduct,
and the daughter-in-law thrills with joy at Mother Shachi's love.

[^20-1]: The end of the summary (on the journey to East Bengal and Lakshmi's departure) is almost illegible in the scan of the edition; it is translated from the surviving words, and gaps are marked […].
[^20-2]: The name of the raga is illegible in the scan of A; raga shri follows the 1903 edition (the 1983 edition has pathamanjari). In the 1983 edition this section is the "Fifth Chapter".
[^20-3]: Vv. 14+1 – 14+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition does not have them.
[^20-4]: Vv. 22+1 – 22+5 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition gives vv. 22+1 – 22+2 in a footnote as the additional reading of one manuscript, and does not have vv. 22+3 – 22+5.
[^20-5]: Thakura ("master") — here the Lord Himself.
[^20-6]: Mahesha — Shiva; Lochana Dasa respectfully calls him, like Narada, "Thakura".
[^20-7]: Shabda-brahman — Brahman as sound, transcendental sound.
[^20-8]: This is the Puranic story of how Vishnu melted on hearing Shiva's song, and so the Ganga was born. The Lord's body is spiritual, and His "melting" is a manifestation of His love for His devotees' singing, not any loss to Him: Bhaktisiddhanta in his summary understands it as the perspiration that appeared on the Lord's body in ecstasy at the kirtana about Him — that moisture is the Ganga.
[^20-9]: I.e. the Ganga is the Lord Himself (Janardana) in the form of sacred water.
[^20-10]: When the Lord as Vamana-Trivikrama strode upward, the nail of His foot pierced the shell of the universe, and the waters of the Causal Ocean — the Ganga — poured in; Brahma washed that foot (Bhagavata 5.17.1, 8.21.1–4). Cf. en/19, vv. 119–120.
[^20-11]: East Bengal was traditionally "the land the Pandavas avoided" (pandava-varjita): in their wanderings the Pandavas did not visit it, and it was held to be impure. The river Padma (Padmavati) is a branch of the Ganga, but the Ganga's sanctity was not acknowledged for it: "the Ganga and yet not the Ganga".
[^20-12]: Damodara — a name of Krishna; cf. v. 86.
[^20-13]: Here (vv. 76–86, 86+1 – 86+2) is again Lochana Dasa's motif of nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9); cf. Murari 1.11.6–10.
[^20-14]: I.e. Gaura is Krishna Himself, Radha's Beloved, who has taken on Radha's complexion and mood; so too the Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition understands Him (cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 1.5).
[^20-15]: Vv. 86+1 – 86+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them, without brackets.
[^20-16]: I.e. the Lord Himself begged people to give Him their sins and take the name of Hari.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 13 (portion 21)

*The end of the Banga-vijaya: the serpent of separation bites Lakshmi, and she departs from this world; Shachi's lament; the Lord returns from East Bengal and consoles His mother with the story of the cursed apsara. The beginning of the second wedding: Shachi seeks the daughter of Sanatana Pandita for her son; the Lord's jest with the astrologer and Sanatana's grief; the Lord dispels his grief*

*Raga vibhasa*

**109.** Thus Shachi lived together with Lakshmi;
but what destiny has ordained cannot be undone.

**110.** Not seeing the Lord, Lakshmi pined at heart:
separation from the Lord was ever present within her.

**111.** Separation took the form of a serpent —
Lakshmi Thakurani knew it in her heart.[^21-1]

**112.** A huge serpent bit Lakshmi on the foot;
in dismay Shachi ponders within herself.

**113.** From the burning pain of the bite Lakshmi writhes;
seeing this, Shachi-devi fell into great distress.

**114.** She called a snake-charmer who knew many mantras
and inquired about many remedies.

**115.** They tried hard, but the poison does not recede;
Shachi was greatly afraid and fell into despair.

**116.** Seeing that her time had come, all gave up their efforts
and, remembering Sri Hari, carried her into the waters of the Ganga.

**117.** They placed a garland of tulasi round her neck;
on all sides all the people chant the name of Hari.

**118.** Lakshmi went to the Lord — the people did not understand it,
but all beheld with their own eyes a supreme wonder:

**119.** the gandharvas brought a chariot along the path of the sky,
and Lakshmi, saying "Hari", left her body and went to heaven.

**120.** A certain shakti, a portion of Lakshmi, went to Vaikuntha;
seeing this, all the people were overwhelmed.

**121.** Lakshmi went to Indra's city, her own abode,[^21-2]
shining with the supreme radiance of Lakshmi, wholly Lakshmi.

**122.** Meanwhile here Shachi-devi weeps in grief;
surrounded by the women, she weeps, recounting [her daughter-in-law's] virtues.

**123.** Tears stream from her eyes, the cloth on her breast is soaked;
striking her head, she heaves hot, heavy sighs:

**124.** "My daughter-in-law Lakshmi was like Lakshmi in every virtue and in nature;
in all Nadia there is none to compare with her.

**125.** How shall I go home alone?
Why have you forgotten your love for me?

**126.** All that was prepared for the worship of the Deities lies there —
why have you gone, leaving your service to me?

**127.** From this day my home is empty;
Vishvambhara married you, but you never stayed at His side.[^21-3]

**128.** O wicked serpent, where were you hiding?
Why did you not devour me, but devoured my daughter-in-law, so full of life?

**129.** Having appointed my daughter-in-law to serve me,
my son went to foreign lands without a care.

**130.** How shall I, unfortunate one, look at my son's face?
What shall I do? My heart burns, not seeing my daughter-in-law."

**131.** Seeing such lamentation, all the relatives and friends
say: "Shachi-devi, compose yourself!

**132.** Who can change what is ordained for anyone?
This whole world is deceptive, and deceptive are all these bodies.[^21-4]

**133.** Who is to instruct you — you know everything;
knowing and hearing all this, why do you not accept consolation?

**134.** Having taken a body, no one escapes death:
even Brahma and all the other gods die.

**135.** Some sooner, some later — death awaits all;
birth and death are the lot of all.

**136.** The one true Reality is Krishna — this we know from the Vedas;
whoever does not worship such a Krishna is a mine of folly."

**137.** Saying this, all the relatives consoled her,
and all together, saying "Hari", restrain their weeping.

**138.** Then all together, having performed the funeral rites
as prescribed, went to their homes.

**139.** Shachi, weeping and weeping, went to her own home;
gathering together, relatives and friends consoled her.

**140.** And Vishvambhara, having stayed there a few more days,
set out for home with a joyful heart.

**141.** With silver, gold, cloth, pearls and coral
all the Vaishnavas honoured Him without measure.

**142.** The Lord came home with many riches
and joyfully gave them to His mother.

**143.** Bowing, the Lord looks at her face:
Shachi, her face downcast, does not say a word.

**144.** Vishvambhara again takes the dust of her feet;
seeing her sad face, He spoke.

**145.** Having given His mother all the wealth He had brought,
the Lord, astonished, says very softly:

**146.** "Why do I see your face so sad?
Seeing you sad, My heart burns."

**147.** Hearing this, Shachi, her voice choking,
began to weep, and her tears soaked the cloth on her breast.

**148.** She can say nothing — her voice is full of grief;
at last she said: "My daughter-in-law has gone to Vaikuntha."

**149.** Hearing this, the Lord grew sad at heart;
His eyes filled with tears of tenderness.

**150.** The Lord said to His mother: "Listen to My words:
I shall tell you the earlier story — why she was born.

**151.** Once an apsara of Indra was dancing,
and by the will of destiny she missed a step.

**152.** The rhythm was broken, and the lord of the gods cursed her:
'Be born on earth in a human home.'

**153.** Having cursed her, the king of the gods again took pity:
'You will not suffer — a great task awaits you.

**154.** On earth the Ishvara will appear as an avatara,
and you will become His wife' — such was the boon he gave.

**155.** 'And then you will return here, to Indra's city.'
That is all about that beauty of Indra.[^21-5]

**156.** Grieve no more, listen, My mother:
what the Creator has written cannot be changed."

**157.** Shachi listened attentively to her son's words,
ceased grieving and thought of it no more.

**158.** Consoled, Shachi turned to other thoughts;
sitting with the devotees, she speaks of her affairs.

**159.** Having said this, Vishvambhara became thoughtful;
concealing Himself, He speaks of various things.[^21-6]

**160.** Says Lochana Dasa: listen to this wonderful
deed of Gauranga — Lakshmi's ascent to heaven.

## Kaishora-lila (The Pastimes of Youth): the Lord's Second Wedding

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* When some days had passed after Lakshmi-devi's ascent to heaven, Mother Shachi grew anxious to have Nimai married again and sent the brahmana Kashinatha to the house of Sanatana Pandita to settle a match with his daughter. When Kashinatha Mishra, coming to Sanatana Pandita's house, conveyed Shachi-devi's words, Sanatana Pandita with the greatest joy agreed to give his daughter to Vishvambhara. An astrologer was called and the day and hour of the wedding were fixed; on the eve of the wedding the respectable ladies and the brahmanas duly performed the popular and the Vedic rites. As before, the anointing with turmeric and the other rites were duly performed. On the wedding day everything was done with great pomp, just as the previous time. After the wedding Sanatana Mishra sent his daughter with his son-in-law to His house.[^21-7]

*Raga gandhara;[^21-8] disha: "Moon of the twice-born line, jewel-like Gora! Nadia rejoices, 'Hari' resounds, cries rise up. O-ki ho re, Gauranga, jaya, jaya!"*

**1.** Thus Lord Vishvambhara, the son of Shachi,
joyfully spent His days in Navadvipa.

**2.** He lived happily with His relatives and friends,
but in Shachi's heart sorrow suddenly arose.

**3.** Seeing the house without a daughter-in-law, she grew very anxious:
"I shall get Vishvambhara married" — so she resolved within.

**4.** Having considered, she came to a decision:
"There is a certain maiden — if only fortune favours us."

**5.** Seeing before her a brahmana named Kashinatha,
Shachi in private opened her heart to him:

**6.** "Go to the house of Sanatana Pandita
and tell him fully what I say:

**7.** my son is endowed with every virtue and good nature;
if it pleases him, He is a worthy bridegroom for his daughter."

**8.** So Shachi spoke to the brahmana,
and, having heard, the brahmana Kashinatha set out quickly.

**9.** Pandita Sanatana was in his house;
there came Kashinatha, the best of brahmanas.

**10.** "Come in, come in!" said [Sanatana], and offered him a seat;
"On what business have you come?" he asks with a smile.

**11.** Kashinatha says: "Listen, listen, pandit:
I shall tell you everything, as is proper.

**12.** You know all the shastras, you are renowned on earth;
what virtue is there unknown to you?

**13.** You are supremely righteous, devoted to Vishnu;
you are a brahmana faithful to his own dharma.

**14.** Knowing this, Shachi, Vishvambhara's mother,
called me and told me what is in her heart.

**15.** She has sent me straight to you;
listen attentively to what I say.

**16.** Regarding you as her own, I tell you her heart;
judge for yourself and do what is fitting:

**17.** Vishvambhara is a worthy bridegroom for your daughter.
I have said everything — give your answer."

**18.** Hearing this, Sanatana Mishra reflected
and with his relatives settled on his word.

**19.** Sanatana says to Kashinatha Pandita:
"I shall open my heart to you, listen, honoured sir.

**20.** This is what I have been thinking day and night,
but I did not dare to say it openly.

**21.** Today is an auspicious day — destiny favours me:
Vishvambhara, the treasure-house of virtues, will become my son-in-law.

**22.** Then indeed I understood how great my good fortune is,
when Shachi-devi herself gave the command.

**23.** Whose fortune can equal mine?
I shall give my daughter to the Parabrahman, Sri Govinda!

**24.** The feet that Brahma and Shiva ever worship —
giving my daughter to them, I too shall worship them."

**25.** As Kashinatha, the best of brahmanas, set out,
[Sanatana] said: "Convey this at the feet of Shachi-devi:

**26.** 'Having fixed the time, I shall send a brahmana;
see to the preparations for the auspicious event.'"

**27.** So Pandita Sri Sanatana replied,
and Kashinatha, the best of brahmanas, went off quickly.

**28.** Coming, he bowed at Shachi's feet
and in her presence related everything.

**29.** Receiving the answer, Shachi was overjoyed
and, smiling, began the preparations for her son's wedding.

**30.** Blessed Shachi gathers all kinds of things,
and on one pretext or another goes to see the maiden.

**31.** And Sanatana, the excellent pandit,
after waiting some days, sent a brahmana:

**32.** "Convey my words at Shachi's feet;
remind her of what the brahmana said before.

**33.** If, to my good fortune, she gives her word,
come back quickly, so that we may set to work here.

**34.** Sri Shachinandana is the Parabrahman, Sri Govinda:
by giving my daughter to Him I shall be freed from samsara."

**35.** Hearing this, the brahmana went to Shachi's house
and, arriving, related everything at Shachi's feet:

**36.** "Pandita Sri Sanatana has sent me
to open his heart to you:

**37.** if, to his good fortune, you, blessed lady, permit it,
he will give his daughter to your son Vishvambhara."

**38.** "Good, good!" said Shachi in great joy.
"I agree; do everything quickly."

**39.** Hearing this, the brahmana in great joy
began to speak sweet words:

**40.** "Vishnupriya will gain a husband like Vishvambhara —
and the name Vishnupriya ('beloved of Vishnu') will be true for her.

**41.** As Rukmini gained Sri Krishna as her husband,
so it will be with her — so my heart tells me."[^21-9]

**42.** Hearing this, Shachi was overjoyed,
and the brahmana went and told everything to the pandit.

**43.** Pandita Sri Sanatana was very pleased
and began to prepare all that is needed for a wedding.

**44.** The wise [pandit] has all kinds of things and ornaments made;
he planned to perform the adhivasa.

**45.** Calling an astrologer, he says humbly:
"I am giving Vishnupriya in marriage — fix the time."

**46.** The astrologer said: "Listen, listen, pandit:
today I unexpectedly met Vishvambhara.

**47.** Seeing Him, my heart rejoiced,
and I said to Him in jest:

**48.** 'Tomorrow is Your auspicious adhivasa,
there will be a wedding — hear what I say.'

**49.** Hearing this, He answered:
'Tell Me, where, whose wedding, who is the bride, who the groom?

**50.** When was this ever spoken of with Me?'
Understand how matters stand and act accordingly."[^21-10]

**51.** Hearing such words from the astrologer,
[Sanatana] took courage and said nothing at the time.

**52.** Sanatana Pandita, a man of noble character,
considered the main point with his relatives:

**53.** "I have prepared all kinds of things, had all kinds of ornaments made;
whom shall I blame? It is my karma.

**54.** I have committed no offence,
yet Gaurahari has without reason rejected [my] love."[^21-11]

**54+1.** Having lost the treasure — the joy of kinship with Gauranga —[^21-12]
he cried "Alas, alas, Gaurachandra!" and fell to the ground.

**54+2.** He weeps aloud, crying: "Hari, Hari!
Not seeing You, Vishvambhara, I am dying!

**54+3.** Glory to Vishvambhara, the deliverer of the fallen!
You fulfilled Bhishmaka's desire in the city of Vidarbha.

**54+4.** Glory to Murari, the guardian of Rukmini's desire!
You brought to Yourself all the beautiful princesses [held captive by] Naraka,

**54+5.** and, knowing their hearts, You married them all.
Marry my daughter too — You are the true dharma!

**54+6.** Do not spurn me in contempt as one fallen:
how many, many fallen ones You have taken up and saved!

**54+7.** Glory to Vishvambhara, the saviour of the people of the world!
Glory to the Lord of all lords, the Creator of the creator!

**54+8.** But I am the lowest of the low, my mind is very dull:
never have I known even the scent of worship of You."

**55.** Sorrow arose in his heart, and he gave voice to it;
and his brahmana wife, her heart aflame, speaks —

**56.** she was of noble birth, modest, well-bred, faithful to her husband,
endowed with every virtue and good nature, devoted to Vishnu —

**57.** seeing her husband's grief, she herself was much grieved
and, setting aside her shyness, speaks before her husband:

**58.** "If Vishvambhara Himself did not wish this match,
what blame can the people of Nadia lay on you?

**59.** If Vishvambhara Hari Himself did not wish it,
what can you do by your own power?

**60.** He is the independent Person, the Lord of all,
whose servants are Brahma, Rudra, Indra and the rest.

**61.** How can He become your son-in-law?
Calm your mind, remember Krishna.

**62.** It is not in our power — there is no point in grieving;
I am afraid to say it, but cast off your grief now."

**63.** When his beloved spoke these words to him,
Pandita Sri Sanatana restrained his grief.

**64.** Together with his relatives he came to this conclusion:
"What is my fault? Vishvambhara did not wish it."

**65.** Having said this, he spoke no more to anyone;
the brahmana and his wife grieved at heart.

**66.** He brooded within, and again sorrow arose:
"Alas, Vishvambhara-deva has put me to shame!

**67.** Glory, glory to You who took away Draupadi's shame and fear!
Glory, glory to You who saved the elephant from the crocodile's jaws![^21-13]

**68.** Saviour of the Pandavas, life of Rukmini!
Glory, glory to You who freed Ahalya from her sin!"

**69.** Thus the best of brahmanas offered many prayers,
and Lord Gauranga, the Lord of the world, came to know of it.

**70.** Hearing all about it, Vishvambhara
thought in His heart: "Why are they grieving so?

**71.** Two of My devotees have become sad at heart,
and yet I said it in jest, laughing."

**72.** Among His friends there was one dear friend;
in private He told him all that was in His mind:

**73.** "On some pretext go to the pandit's house
and speak as though I knew nothing of it:

**74.** 'It was in jest that I said this to the astrologer —
why then, without understanding, did [you] give up the matter?

**75.** There is no reason at all to give up the matter;
it is not right that the two of them should grieve.

**76.** What His mother has said — what more is there to discuss?
Who would go against her word?

**77.** The matter suffers for nothing, you grieve for nothing;
do all that is proper for the wedding.'"

**78.** Having so instructed him, the Lord sent the brahmana,
and he related everything to Sanatana Pandita.

[^21-1]: Cf. en/17, note 4: Lakshmipriya is Lakshmi herself. Lochana Dasa presents the serpent's bite as separation from the Lord that has taken the form of a serpent — and so does Bhaktisiddhanta in his summary: "bitten by the serpent of separation". Her departure is not a mundane death: she goes to the Lord (v. 118). Cf. Murari 1.11.21–28.
[^21-2]: The 1983 edition has it the other way round: a portion of Lakshmi went to heaven (svarga), and Lakshmi herself to Vaikuntha, "her own abode". In A Indra's city is called her abode in agreement with the Lord's story of the apsara (vv. 151–155). Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Murari (1.12.17) and Vrindavana Dasa, see in Lakshmipriya Lakshmi herself, who went to the feet of her Lord; the story of the apsara is the words with which the Lord consoled His mother while concealing Himself (cf. v. 159).
[^21-3]: The 1983 edition: "having married, Vishvambhara went to foreign lands".
[^21-4]: "Mithya" ("deceptive, false") here refers to the transience of worldly life and of bodies, not to the unreality of the world in the Mayavada sense; the one eternal Reality is Krishna (v. 136).
[^21-5]: Cf. Murari 1.12.13–17, where the Lord tells the same story "concealing everything with words strong to hide His own self" and adds: "Or else it was the Mistress of the world herself, in the form of Lakshmi, who went to the lotus feet of her Lord". See note 2.
[^21-6]: I.e. without revealing His divinity. V. 158 is absent in the 1983 edition.
[^21-7]: Bhaktisiddhanta's summary does not include the episode with the astrologer (vv. 45–78); in the scan this summary is damaged in places and is translated from the surviving text.
[^21-8]: The name of the raga is illegible in the scan of A; raga gandhara follows the 1983 edition (where this is the "Sixth Chapter").
[^21-9]: Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Vishnupriya as the embodiment of Bhu-shakti, the Lord's energy. Cf. Murari 1.13.13–15.
[^21-10]: The Lord's jest: He pretends to know nothing of the wedding. Cf. Murari 1.13.18–21. The second line is the astrologer's advice to Sanatana.
[^21-11]: Cf. Murari 1.13.22–30.
[^21-12]: Vv. 54+1 – 54+8 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition gives them in a footnote as the additional reading of one manuscript. Bhishmaka is the king of Vidarbha, Rukmini's father; Rukmini longed to become Krishna's wife, and He carried her away (Bhagavata 10.52–54). Naraka (Bhaumasura) held sixteen thousand princesses captive; Krishna, having slain him, freed them and married them (10.59).
[^21-13]: On Draupadi, Gajendra and the others see en/17, note 6. Ahalya — the wife of the sage Gautama, freed from a curse by the touch of Rama's foot.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 14 (portion 22)

*The second wedding (conclusion): the adhivasa of the Lord and of Vishnupriya; the wedding morning; the bridegroom's attire and the bride's beauty; the wedding procession and the excitement of the women of Nadia; the welcoming of the bridegroom, the exchange of glances and the giving away of the bride in Sanatana's house; the wedding night, the kushandika, Sanatana's farewell; Shachi welcomes the couple home*

*Raga ramakeli; disha: "Hari, Rama, Narayana — Shachi's darling, golden Gora!"*

**79.** Then the pandit, with great joy in his heart,
happily fixes an auspicious day and an auspicious moment.

**80.** And here the Lord Vishvambhara, learning of it,
called an astrologer to His house and fixes the auspicious day.

**81.** After deliberation they fixed the day, a wonderful time:
an auspicious hour, an auspicious lagna, tithi and a favourable nakshatra[^22-1].

**82.** At the time of the adhivasa, holy men, brahmanas and good people
gathered and arranged for the Lord all that the auspicious rite required.

**83.** Joyful Shachi-devi, gathering the married women,
celebrates her son's festival, giving all kinds of gifts:

**84.** oil, turmeric and sindura for the forehead,
puffed rice, bananas, sweets and betel.

**85.** Joyfully all the married women sing auspicious songs;
all the brahmanas perform the Lord's adhivasa.

**86.** All the quarters are adorned with incense, lamps and banners;
after the svastivachana blessings they worship the gods.

**87.** The brahmanas recite the Vedas, auspicious conches sound,
all kinds of instruments play — pataha drums and mridangas.

**88.** On all sides the wives of noble families cry "Jaya, jaya!";
the Lord's adhivasa fell at an excellent hour.

**89.** The brahmanas were honoured with scents, sandal and garlands,
with camphor, betel and many ornaments.

**90.** At this time the venerable pandit Sanatana,
full of reverence, his heart rejoicing,

**91.** sent brahmanas and virtuous brahmana wives,
wishing to perform the adhivasa of his son-in-law,

**92.** while he himself performs the adhivasa of his daughter;
her body glitters with jewelled ornaments.

**93.** He duly performs the worship of the gods and the ancestors;
throughout the adhivasa "Jaya, jaya!" resounds without cease.

**94.** The brahmanas recite the Vedas, auspicious conches sound,
dundubhis joyfully resound, mridangas play.

**95.** Thus the adhivasa of the two was performed;
on the next day the Lord rose at dawn[^22-2].

**95+1.** All kinds of instruments sound, cries of "Jaya!" and "ulu-ulu";
full of rasa, the women went along, swaying[^22-3].

**95+2.** Rapt in rasa, how many feelings rise in their hearts!
The sweetness of Gauranga is what their hearts have gained.

**95+3.** On the moonlit night [rang] auspicious songs;
all that is proper for Vishnupriya's wedding they performed.

**95+4.** Thus, having fetched the water, the wives of noble families
came at dawn to Shachi's house.

**96.** After His morning rites the Lord bathed in the Ganga
and performed the nandimukha-shraddha as prescribed.

**97.** Having completed the worship of the gods and the ancestors,
the Lord bathes again, as is proper before a wedding.

**98.** Then the barber did his work,
and the wives of noble families anoint His body.

**98+1.** They apply fragrant amalaki, oil and turmeric;
one, touching His divine body, sank into a swoon of bliss[^22-4].

**98+2.** Another joyfully massages His feet;
another's face is uncovered — where has shame gone?

**98+3.** From the eyes of some flow tears of joy,
the bodies of others tremble at the breeze from His body.

**98+4.** The frenzied women bathe [the Lord],
openly showing the long-cherished wish of their hearts.

**98+5.** One, leaning, sinks [against Him], another pours Ganga water;
cries of "Jaya!" and "ulu-ulu" are heard with auspicious shouts.

**99.** In the town of Nadia there is joy and jubilation:
Vishvambhara's wedding is an all-auspicious festival.

**100.** Then Mahaprabhu Vishvambhara Raya
adorns His body with all that is fitting:

**101.** divine jewelled ornaments, a cloth with a red border;
the breeze from Gora's body spreads fragrance.

**102.** The natural fragrance of His divine body — and divine perfumes besides;
a sandal tilaka on the forehead, and the beautiful moon-like face.

**103.** The nails shine like moons, rings are on the fingers;
the radiance of His body dazzles — one cannot look at it.

**104.** The soft red lips are like bimba fruits;
by the ears the cheeks shine like bandhuka flowers.

**105.** Armlets and bracelets on the arms, anklets on the feet;
seeing Him, the nagaris tremble at heart[^22-5].

**105+1.** All the nagaris surrounded Gauranga —
as the stars in their beauty encircle the moon[^22-6].

**105+2.** All the women grew drunk with the wine of Madana;
casting off shame and fear, they stood gazing at His face.

**106.** And here, in his own house, the pandit Sri Sanatana
adorns his daughter with jewelled ornaments.

**107.** She was arrayed with scents, sandal and garlands;
yet even without adornment the radiance of her body lit up the land.

**108.** Vishnupriya's body surpasses the purest gold;
she glitters like lightning that has taken form.

**109.** Her braid surpasses a serpent and captivates the minds of sages;
the sindura on her forehead — to what can it be compared?

**110.** The curve of her brows is lovely as Ananga's bow;
her nose surpasses a parrot's beak — it is supremely beautiful.

**111.** Her eyes surpass the eyes of a doe;
her charming ears surpass the ears of a vulture[^22-7].

**112.** Her lips surpass bandhuli flowers — a peerless beauty;
her teeth surpass pearls — a glittering lustre.

**113.** Her throat surpasses the conch, enchanting the world;
surpassing the lion's neck, beautiful is her neck.

**114.** Her arms surpass in beauty golden lotus stalks;
her palms, one would think, surpass red lotuses.

**115.** Her fingers are lovelier than champaka buds;
her nails surpass the moon, their beauty glitters.

**116.** The breadth of her bosom surpasses Sumeru;
her waist surpasses the lion's — so very slender.

**117.** Her hips surpass the wheel of Kamadeva's chariot,
her thighs the trunks of the ramakadali plantain.

**118.** The Creator fashioned a beauty surpassing the three worlds;
the soles of her feet, red as the lotus, glow.

**119.** The row of her moon-like nails surpasses the spotless moon;
by their rays even one blind from birth received sight.

**120.** She was arrayed with scents, sandal and garlands;
yet even without adornment the radiance of her body lights up the land.

**121.** The maiden who enchants the three worlds surpasses Parvati;
by her ornaments the earth glitters.

**122.** Then, seeing that the auspicious hour had come,
[Sanatana] sent a brahmana to bring the bridegroom.

**123.** The brahmana stood before the Lord
and humbly says: "The brahmana [Sanatana] has sent me."

**124.** Seeing the dazzling radiance of His body, the brahmana
considers himself blessed: "Blessed is Sanatana too!"

**125.** He said before the Lord: "Listen, Vishvambhara:
the auspicious hour is near — come quickly.

**126.** What can I say in Your presence?
You are God, Bhagavan: I see it with my own eyes."

**127.** Then at that auspicious moment the Lord Vishvambhara
set out in a palanquin, gently smiling.

**128.** Shachi with the married women gives her blessing;
the Lord took the dust of His mother's feet upon His head.

**129.** Conches, dundubhis, bheurs and kahals sound,
dandimas and muharis sound, dindimas sound sweetly[^22-8].

**130.** Vinas, flutes, kapilasas, rababs, upangas
and pakhoyajs all play together.

**131.** Patahas and mridangas, bronze cymbals and karatalas sound,
shingas and bargos sound together with the sahinis.

**132.** All kinds of instruments sound — I do not know their names;
in front dancers dance, the chanting of the Vedas is heard.

**133.** Singers sing songs, the bhat bards sing praises;
surrounded by His friends, the Lord moved forward.

**134.** In the town of Nadia every house was astir;
people run to look, waving their arms.

*Raga bihagara*

**135.** They put on silk saris and bodices of fine cloth,
tie their hair in a knot in the kanada style;
stringing pearls and setting them in gold,
they let red tassels fall down their backs.

**136.** The beauties, the beauties — the ladies of Nadia —
are carried away into an ocean of joy:
"Let us go and see Vishvambhara's wedding,
we will sing auspicious songs!"

**137.** Some put on garments, silk saris,
gandharaja and champaka flowers in their ears;
they move with an elephant's stately gait — they cannot walk fast —
and glance sidelong with the eyes of a doe.

**138.** Their wagtail eyes, tinged with kajal, —
a restless pair of pupils —
have sunk in the mire of Gora's beauty
and, languid and entranced, will move no more.

**139.** In every quarter all the ladies of the town,
hearing the sound, ran out;
young girls ran with combs in their hair,
forgetting to arrange their clothes.

**140.** Young women, abandoning thoughts of their husbands,
leaving their families and kin,
do not arrange their clothes or ornaments,
as though forever maddened[^22-9].

**141.** Like motionless lightning — such is their gait,
the gait of she-swans;
row upon row, holding hands,
they are like autumn moons.

**142.** Women or men — all run in one direction,
no one heeds anyone;
jostling on the road, they run in a frenzy
to see the face of Gauranga.

**142+1.** The town of Nadia is an ocean of joy,
Gauranga is the treasure-nagara;
on all sides people run, festive music sounds —
[the women] are like playful does[^22-10].

**143.** Children, the old, the blind, the lame, the crippled,
the sick look on with longing;
some, joining hands with their friends,
run without tying their clothes.

**144.** Seeing His face, the women, in the pangs of Madana,
lose their composure;
all the beasts and birds, seeing Gauranga,
stand still, row upon row.

**145.** Surrounded by friends, divinely adorned,
a crown just above the forehead…
Lochana says: seeing [Him], the nagaris lost their heads,
the doors of their hearts flew open.

*Raga varadi; tune "dhulakhela"[^22-11]*

**146.** Thus Vishvambhara came to the pandit's house;
the best of brahmanas was in an ocean of joy.
Taking in his hands water for the feet and arghya,
he went out to meet the Lord: "Blessed, blessed is Shachi's son!"

**147.** Offering water for the feet and arghya, he led Gaurachandra in,
and He stood on the wedding platform.
All cry "Hari!", hundreds upon hundreds of lamps burn,
but Gaura's body outshines them.

**148.** The married women rejoice, again and again the "ulu-ulu" cries ring out,
conches, dundubhis and instruments sound;
and there the married women, gathering — some in silk saris —
adorn themselves to circle round the Lord.

**149.** Having prepared all for the nirmanchhana, ahead of the married women
comes the mother of the bride;
her feet do not touch the ground, her whole body rejoices
at the sight of Vishvambhara, the jewel of virtues.

**150.** Thinking of Gaurahari, filling her heart with Him —
how many longings rise in her heart! —
"Vishnupriya, my daughter, will be a match for Him":
