thinking so, she restrained her thoughts[^22-12].

**151.** Not only do the married women shine with beauty, with jewelled lamps in their hands, —
on top of that come the rays of Gora's body;
by the fragrance of that body the women all but die in frenzy
and with great effort hold their hearts in check.

**152.** Having circled Gorachand seven times and welcomed the bridegroom,
they pour curd on His lotus feet;
when the time comes to go back into the house, they gaze at Gora's face —
and cannot turn away: [they are held by] the fragrance of His body.

**153.** The pandit Sri Sanatana honours the bridegroom:
divine garments, divine ornaments;
with divine scents and sandal he anoints His body,
he placed a garland of malati round His neck.

**154.** His body is beautiful as Sumeru — and as if the heavenly Ganga,
dividing in two, were flowing in two streams:
a garland of malati on the body of Gora!
Seeing this, the pandit thrilled all over with joy[^22-13].

**155.** Then Sanatana Mishra, jewel among brahmanas,
ordered his daughter to be brought;
the beauty of the three worlds sits on a jewelled throne —
from the radiance of her body it was as if lightning flashed.

**156.** They brought her to the Lord — she who enchants the mind of the world,
Vishnupriya, whose name is Mahalakshmi[^22-14];
with tremulous, playful eyes she looks at Gauranga's face
with a gentle, peerless smile.

**157.** Circling the Lord, going round Him seven times,
she bows [to Him] with joined palms;
the screen between them was removed — four eyes met;
both shower each other with flowers.

**158.** Cries of joy arose, all repeat: "Hari, Hari!";
the bridegroom and bride waved the chhamuni[^22-15];
all say: "Wonderful, wonderful! — like the Moon and Rohini";
some say: "Parvati and Shankara."

**159.** Then the Lord Vishvambhara, gently smiling,
sat on an excellent throne;
Sanatana, the best of brahmanas, gives away his daughter,
offering her at His lotus feet.

**160.** All that was fitting — all kinds of gifts — he presented;
the two sat together.
After the wedding the two, in the house of the brahmana Sanatana,
ate together.

**161.** The joyful married women plot among themselves,
taking betel and camphor in their hands;
they gazed their fill at Sri Gaurangachand Hari, —
the Thakura sat in the bridal chamber.

**162.** Vishvambhara and Vishnupriya went and sat in the bridal chamber,
and the married women think to themselves:
"This Vishnupriya is Lakshmi, and Vishnu has become Vishvambhara —
thus They have come to the earth."

**163.** Those skilled in all the arts, taking divine garlands in their hands,
put them round Vishvambhara's neck;
some voice the desire that was in their hearts:
"This is my secret: I have sold myself to You"[^22-16].

**164.** Some anoint His body with scents and sandal —
at the touch their frenzy grows;
starting all sorts of talk, they lean against His body —
thus the desire of their whole life was fulfilled.

**164+1.** All the beauties who were there grew frenzied
and openly speak their hearts' secrets;
rapt in rasa they laugh and, swaying, lean against Gora,
maddened, overwhelmed by desire (kama)[^22-17].

**165.** One, filling a tray with betel, sets it at the Lord's feet
and offers a handful of flowers;
this is what is in her heart: "You are my Lord birth after birth" —
saying so, she gives herself [to Him].

**166.** Thus the jewel of virtues spent the night —
the good fortune of the married women was revealed;
in the morning, rising, the Lord, the treasury of virtues,
duly performed that same day the rite of kushandika[^22-18].

**167.** On the next day the Lord, seating the bride at His left,
said: "I shall go home."
The household honour [Him] — each as he wished;
cries of "Jaya, jaya!" and the sound of conches arose.

**168.** The brahmana Sanatana and his wife, placing in [Their] hands
betel nuts, sandal and garlands,
lay durva grass and rice on Their heads, wish Them good and well-being
and bless Them: "Live long!"

**169.** Then the heart of Vishnupriya-devi trembled,
she looks into the faces of her father and mother;
and [they], in a voice full of tears, give themselves over [to the Lord]
with humble entreaty.

**170.** Sanatana, the best of brahmanas, says with an aching heart:
"What can I say to You?
By Your own goodness You have accepted my daughter —
what worthy of You can I give?

**171.** And I say this too: You are my son-in-law —
blessed am I, blessed is my house;
blessed is my Vishnupriya, having attained Your lotus feet" —
saying this, he is choked with emotion.

**172.** His eyes brim with tears, his face is flushed,
he speaks haltingly, faltering;
taking Vishnupriya's hand, he placed it in Vishvambhara's hand,
and tears stream from his eyes.

**173.** Then at the auspicious moment the Lord mounted the palanquin —
the hearts of all rejoice;
all kinds of instruments sound, conches and dundubhis roar,
cries of "Hari!" touch the sky.

**174.** In front dancers dance; whatever skill anyone has,
all display it at once;
the Lord rides in the palanquin, people cry "Jaya, jaya!" —
and He arrived at His own home.

**175.** Shachi, rejoicing, taking all that was needed for the nirmanchhana,
together with the married women —
amid auspicious cries of "Jaya, jaya!", while all repeat "Hari!" —
circles [the couple] with all kinds of gifts and casts them away[^22-19].

**176.** In front is the auspicious pot, the bhat bards sing praises,
the brahmanas chant the Vedas;
holding Vishnupriya by the hand, Vishvambhara Gaurahari
enters the house at the auspicious moment.

**177.** Overwhelmed with love and joy, embracing Vishvambhara,
[Shachi] kisses His moon-like face;
beside herself with joy, she goes among the married women
and dances, holding her daughter-in-law in her arms.

**178.** Forgetting herself in happiness, she gives people all kinds of gifts,
and everyone was satisfied.
Seeing Vishvambhara and Vishnupriya together,
Lochana sings the glories of Gora[^22-20].

*Thus ends the description of the second wedding.*

[^22-1]: Lagna — the ascendant sign of the zodiac at the moment of the rite; tithi — the lunar day; nakshatra — the lunar asterism. Cf. Murari 1.14.3.
[^22-2]: In the 1983 edition the second line is different: "towards the end of the night the wives went to fetch water" (cf. en/18, note 1); we follow A.
[^22-3]: Vv. 95+1 – 95+4 (the fetching of water) are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition gives them in a note as the additional reading of a manuscript and a printed edition.
[^22-4]: Vv. 98+1 – 98+5 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them, without brackets. Lochana Dasa's motif of nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9; cf. en/18, vv. 40+1 – 40+9).
[^22-5]: Lochana Dasa's motif of nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9).
[^22-6]: Vv. 105+1 – 105+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them.
[^22-7]: A traditional comparison of Bengali poetry: the graceful curve of the ear is likened to the ear of a vulture (gridhini).
[^22-8]: On the bheur, kahal, shinga, bargo, muhari and the others see en/18, note 11, and the glossary. The meaning of the word "dandima" (so in A and B) is unclear.
[^22-9]: Vv. 135–145 (and 142+1) — again Lochana Dasa's motif of nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9; en/18, note 5).
[^22-10]: Stanza 142+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has it, without brackets.
[^22-11]: A has "বরাড়ি রাগ — ধূলাখেলা-জাত"; probably an indication of the tune to which the song is sung (so also in the 1983 and 1903 editions).
[^22-12]: This stanza is absent in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives it in a note as an additional reading of the printed edition. The bride's mother, seeing the bridegroom, rejoices that her daughter is a worthy match for Him and restrains her excitement.
[^22-13]: The white garland on the Lord's golden body is like the Ganga running down the golden mountain Sumeru in two streams; cf. Murari 1.14.22–23 and en/19, v. 100.
[^22-14]: So Lochana Dasa names Vishnupriya; cf. v. 162, where the married women recognize in Vishnupriya Lakshmi and in Vishvambhara Vishnu. In the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding Vishnupriya is the Lord's Bhu-shakti, Lakshmipriya His Shri-shakti (see en/21, note 9).
[^22-15]: On the word "chhamuni" see en/19, note 5.
[^22-16]: Vv. 161–165 are the scene in the bridal chamber (basara, see en/19, note 11), where by custom the women joke with the bridegroom; in Lochana Dasa it is again the motif of nagari-bhava (see en/09, note 9).
[^22-17]: Stanza 164+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has it. "Kama" (desire) is the word by which the scriptures speak also of the gopis' love; the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Adi 4.164–171) explains that such "kama" is pure prema, the desire to please Krishna, not oneself. Lochana Dasa transfers these images to the women of Nadia (nagari-bhava; see en/09, note 9).
[^22-18]: Kushandika — the rite at the sacred fire that concludes the wedding (see en/18, note 2).
[^22-19]: Nichhani — auspicious objects are circled around the couple and cast away, warding off all that is inauspicious; cf. the nirmanchhana (en/19, note 17).
[^22-20]: Cf. Murari 1.14.26–33. In A the last two numbers are printed as "১৭০" and "১৭৬"; 177 and 178 are restored.

---

# Adi-khanda, part 15 (portion 23)

*The journey to Gaya: the Lord teaches and resolves to perform the rite for His father; farewell to Shachi; on the way all hasten to see Him; the lesson of the deer; on Mount Mandara — a fever cured by the water of a brahmana's feet, and the teaching about brahmanas; the meeting with Ishvara Puri and initiation; the moods of Vraja; the rites in Gaya, prema at the Vishnupada; the wish to go to Vrindavana and the heavenly voice; the return to Nadia; the conclusion of the Adi-khanda*

## Kaishora-lila (Youth): the Lord's journey to Gaya

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* After some time Gaurasundara, having concluded the lila of studying, took up teaching. Then, on the pretext of offering pinda for His father, He graciously set out for Gaya. On the way He gave His darshan to all the beasts and birds and drew them to His feet; and to teach reverence for brahmanas He enacted the lila of being cured of His illness by drinking the water that had washed a brahmana's feet, and He also taught that one who does not worship Krishna can never be a brahmana. Then, coming to Gaya and worshipping the gods and the ancestors, He went to see the Vishnupada. There He met Ishvara Puri, the best of devotees, and, to show him mercy, He asked him for a mantra. As soon as the Lord received the Krishna mantra from Ishvara Puri, bhava arose in Him, and He became eager to see the Vishnupada. Seeing the Vishnupada, Sriman Mahaprabhu, overwhelmed with prema, displayed laughter, dancing, singing and other signs of prema, and, staying there only a few days, returned home[^23-1].

*Raga varadi; disha: "My life — Dvijachand!"*

**1.** Thus Mahaprabhu, in joy and delight,
lived happily together with His relatives and friends.

**2.** All the brahmanas living in Navadvipa
say to one another: "Blessed, blessed!"

**3.** The students study the rules of the auspicious worldly rites —
they are taught by the Lord Himself, the jewel among men.

**4.** He surpasses Brihaspati, He is a poet who knows the taste of poetry;
He is Ishvara Himself: with what words can He be praised?

**5.** Who can describe the greatness of the students
whom the Teacher of the world Himself teaches?

**6.** The Lord Vishvambhara, beloved of millions of Sarasvatis,
grants all the pandits His mercy — the taste of learning.

**7.** Thus Vishvambhara instructed the people;
He resolved: "I shall go to perform the rite in Gaya[^23-2].

**8.** I shall offer pinda for My father on the head of Gaya
and bow to Gadadhara and to the Vishnupada."

**9.** Saying this, the Thakura set out on the auspicious journey;
with Him went brahmanas of noble families.

**10.** Shachi's heart burns, her voice falters;
coming to her son, she sighs heavily:

**11.** "You are going to foreign lands — listen, Vishvambhara:
without You there is darkness in my house.

**12.** You are the blind woman's staff, the pupil of my eyes,
the soul of this body — I have no one but You.

**13.** You are going to deliver the ancestors —
what shall I say to You for my own sake?

**13+1.** If You go to Gaya, son — listen, Nimai —
offer there one pinda in my name too"[^23-3].

**14.** When mother Shachi spoke such words,
He consoled her with sweet speech:

**15.** "Know, mother, that I am always
near you" — so He replied.

**16.** "All people need a son for the sake of the pinda;
kindly give Me leave — do not grieve".

**17.** Thus Vishvambhara set out to perform the rite in Gaya;
with Him went brahmanas with joy in their hearts.

**18.** Whatever road the Lord, the son of Shachi, travelled,
the people on that road, seeing Him, delight their eyes.

**19.** Children, the old, the lame, the infirm run to see;
all the beasts and birds run — tears stream from their eyes.

**20.** All the wives of noble families run, forgetting their families;
all say: "Look — here is Sri Hari of Vraja!"

**21.** Saying this, people run without tying their hair —
the Lord, passing through all lands, drove everyone mad.

**22.** Thus on every road all the people run —
He plunges all into the ocean of the rasa of prema.

**23.** Going along the road, Gaurahari sees in one place
a buck and a doe frolicking together.

**24.** Seeing the play of the deer, He was amused
and, like an ordinary man, laughed heartily.

**25.** Beasts are intoxicated with greed, delusion, lust and anger;
so are all people if they do not worship Krishna.

**26.** Smiling, Bhagavan explains to His companions:
"The same intelligence that beasts have is present in men;

**27.** only in the body of a beast there is no knowledge of Krishna —
and a man who does not worship Krishna is called a beast."

**28.** Having explained all this, the Lord, the Teacher of the world,
the Lord who is the Wish-fulfilling Tree, went on His way.

**29.** Then — a river named Chira;
there the Lord bathed and gave gifts as prescribed[^23-4].

**30.** Joyfully performing the worship of the gods and the ancestors,
the Lord climbed Mandara to see the Deity.

**31.** Having seen the Deity, the Lord quickly came down;
at the foot of the mountain He stayed in a brahmana's house.

**32.** Then the brahmanas accompanying Vishvambhara,
seeing the brahmanas of that land, found fault with them in their minds.

**33.** These followed the customs of their land as was their way;
seeing this, [the Lord's companions] did not regard them as brahmanas[^23-5].

**34.** Seeing this disregard for brahmanas, the Lord Vishvambhara
resolved to show reverence for brahmanas.

**35.** Suddenly a high fever seized the Lord's body;
seeing the fever, all were frightened at heart[^23-6].

**36.** The Thakura said: "Listen, listen, all of you:
for what reason has an obstacle arisen to the rite for the gods and the ancestors?

**37.** I do not know — through My fault or through the fault of My companions —
an obstacle has arisen to the auspicious work, and this grieves Me greatly.

**38.** There is a remedy that removes all obstacles:
give Me the water that has washed a brahmana's feet — it will bring relief.

**39.** The water of a brahmana's feet, when drunk, destroys all sins —
the fever will go at once: what can it do?"

**40.** A local brahmana was there;
[the Lord] rose Himself and washed his feet.

**41.** Vishvambhara drank the water of the brahmana's feet,
showed reverence for brahmanas — and the fever fled.

**42.** Then the abashed best of brahmanas speaks humble words:
"Because of the fault in my heart You have suffered pain.

**43.** Seeing [their] customs as bad, my mind found fault [with them];
because of the fault in my mind You were displeased.

**44.** Now You have shown reverence for brahmanas;
I have committed an offence — You, Lord, forgive me.

**45.** You are Brahmanya, You are the guardian of reverence for brahmanas,
bearing on Your chest the mark of the sage Bhrigu's foot[^23-7].

**46.** With Your own lips You reveal the glory of devotion to You
and thus deliver the world.

**47.** Glory to You, beloved Vishvambhara! Glory to You, king of brahmanas!
For one who serves You every undertaking succeeds.

**48.** Obeisance to You, merciful Gaurahari, lover of brahmanas!
Obeisance to You, establisher of dharma, Lord of all!"

**49.** Hearing such words from His companion, Vishvambhara
forgave all of them their many faults.

**50.** "They worship Thakura Madhusudana;
they are not to be rejected — do not regard them as outsiders.

**51.** One who does not worship Krishna is never twice-born —
this teaching is confirmed by the Puranas."

*Thus [it is said][^23-8]:*

**52.** "Even a chandala devoted to Vishnu is better than a sage;
but a twice-born devoid of devotion to Vishnu is lower than a dog-eater."

**53.** Saying this, He was pleased with His brahmana companions
and graciously forgave them their fault.

**54.** Having thus shown reverence for brahmanas,
[the Lord] came to the holy place on the river Punahpuna.

**55.** Performing there His bath and the worship of the Deity,
and completing the rites for the ancestors, He went on.

**56.** Then — the excellent holy place called Rajagiri:
coming to the Brahma-kunda, the Lord bathed and gave gifts[^23-9].

**57.** Having performed there the worship of the gods and the ancestors,
He hastened to see the Vishnupada.

**58.** On the way He saw the best of sannyasis,
a maha-bhagavata named Ishvara Puri.

**59.** Bowing to him, Vishvambhara said:
"By great good fortune I have seen these two feet!"

**60.** Falling at his feet, He speaks plaintive words;
His eyes, reddened and full of entreaty, fill with tears:

**61.** "How shall I cross this ocean of samsara?
Grant Me devotion to Krishna's lotus feet.

**62.** Without initiation into the Krishna mantra the body, I see, is useless:
the words of the Puranas and the lips of the saints all bear witness to this"[^23-10].

**63.** Hearing such words, Ishvara Puri
in private imparted to Him the great, the best of mantras.

**64.** Vishvambhara received the great mantra of Gopinatha[^23-11],
His whole body thrilled, His heart rejoiced.

**65.** Tears flow from His eyes, His body trembles;
repeating "Radha, Radha!", He stirs up waves of happiness.

**66.** All the moods of Vraja awoke in His mind,
and above all His mind plunged into madhurya-rasa.

**67.** His whole body overcome by the mood of Radha,
He calls in a loud voice: "Krishna, Krishna!"

**68.** Calling "Vrindavana, Govardhana!", He laughs;
crying "Kalindi, Yamuna!", He shouts in delight.

**69.** Now He calls Balarama, Shridama, Sudama,
now He calls out the names of Nanda and Yashoda.

**70.** "Dhavali, Shangali!" — He calls in a deep voice;
now, crying "Friend!", the Lord falls down in agitation.

**71.** Now in the mood of a servant He takes a straw between His teeth,
now proudly says: "It is I!

**72.** I held up the mountain, I killed Aghasura,
I killed Putana and all the other asuras!"

**73.** Now, standing in the threefold-bending pose, He holds a flute to His lips,
now, startled, He looks around in all directions[^23-12].

**74.** Tears flow from His eyes, His voice falters;
with sweet words He addresses the guru:

**75.** "By your mercy I have attained the goal:
from this day My embodiment has become meaningful."

**75+1.** Hearing this, Ishvara Puri, in his own bliss,
sees the Lord standing in the threefold-bent pose, with the flute at His lips[^23-13].

**75+2.** He remembered the words of Madhavendra Puri
and understood: Krishnachandra has now appeared.

**76.** Having [thus] shown devotion to the guru, the Lord went on;
seeing the river named Phalgu, He gently smiled.

**77.** He remembered the past — in joy and sorrow;
remembering Sita, He fell into great agitation[^23-14].

**78.** He worshipped the gods and the ancestors, bathed and gave gifts,
and duly offered pinda on the Pretashila.

**79.** For His father He gave wealth to the brahmanas;
having performed [the rite at] Udichi, He performed it at Dakshina-manasa.

**80.** Performing [the rite] at Uttara-manasa and at the holy place Jihvalola,
worshipping the gods and the ancestors, He distributed wealth[^23-15].

**81.** Then with great joy in His heart He arrived in Gaya;
His longing to see the foot of Vishnu grew.

**82.** The Lord offers pindas at the Sixteen Altars;
His eagerness to see the Vishnupada grew.

**83.** Having finished all the rites, He went quickly,
with a joyful heart, to see the Vishnupada.

**84.** "With My own eyes I shall see the marks of Vishnu's foot!" —
joyfully He says to Himself what is in His heart.

**85.** Thinking so, He came to the Vishnupada
and in supreme bliss, having bowed down, sat.

**86.** Gauranga says: "Listen, listen, all of you:
what happens in the heart when one sees the Vishnupada?

**87.** I have seen with My own eyes the marks of Vishnu's foot —
why then, having seen them, has prema not arisen?"[^23-16]

**88.** Saying this, Mahaprabhu washed the Vishnupada,
performed abhisheka — and His heart was filled with joy.

**89.** Showing bhakti, the Lord Vishvambhara Hari —
Gora, the possessor of prema — manifests [it]:

**90.** prema began — trembling, hair standing on end,
streams flow from His eyes, at times He becomes stunned.

**91.** Seeing the lotus foot, the Lord lost Himself
and in a great festival of prema moves about, dancing.

**92.** He offered pinda on the head of Gaya, on the lotus foot;
in joy all the brahmanas dance together [with Him].

**93.** On the next day the secret desire took firm hold in His heart:
the Lord suddenly set out for Madhupuri.

**94.** He said to His brahmana companions:
"Let us go to see Vrindavana."

**95.** Hearing this, the companions were dismayed:
"We cannot go — our means are small."

**96.** The Lord says: "From birth man is bound by the care for food;
not understanding [life's goal], he anxiously performs many works.

**97.** Human birth becomes meaningful if one worships Krishna;
but not worshipping Krishna, one sinks in an ocean of suffering."

**98.** Having explained all this, the Lord Gaurahari
set out from Gaya for Vrindavana.

**99.** He Himself set out together with His companions;
and then a voice rang out from the sky,

**100.** like the deep thunder of a fresh cloud;
addressing Vishvambhara, it said:

**101.** "Listen, listen, Mahaprabhu, O Vishvambhara:
do not go to Vrindavana — go home.

**102.** Having taken sannyasa, You will travel to the holy places
and in due time You will go to Vrindavana"[^23-17].

**103.** Hearing such a heavenly voice with their own ears,
the brahmana companions held Him back from going.

**104.** Turning back, Mahaprabhu went home
and little by little, on foot, came to Nadia.

**105.** Bowing at the feet of His mother Shachi,
He sent all His companions to their homes.

**106.** Shachi, with joy in her heart, embraced her son;
tears of love and joy stream from both her eyes.

**107.** Her whole body thrills, her body trembles;
the whole town of Nadia received joy.

**108.** In Vishnupriya's heart there are waves of joy:
she cannot contain herself, her happiness has no limit.

**109.** With joy the Lord came to His own abode —
happily this Lochana Dasa sings the glories of Gora.

*Raga varadi; disha: "Dvijachand!..." (murchha)*

**110.** The deeds [of the Lord] in Navadvipa are a wondrous tale:
the account of Gorachand's glories is steeped in nectar.

**111.** The deeds in Nadia are beyond the reach of the world and of the Vedas;
hearing them is auspicious — all love them.

**112.** Shiva, Shuka, Narada, Lakshmi, Ananta,
tasting this joy, consider themselves fortunate.

**113.** What can I say, wretched and utterly without understanding?
I know neither good nor bad, neither day nor night.

**114.** My conduct is like that of a beast,
therefore I write of myself that I am the lowest.

**115.** The essence of all avataras is the avatara of Gora,
and in it — the preaching of prema in Nadia.

**116.** Bowing down, I beseech the feet of the Vaishnavas:
be merciful — let my lips sing the glories of Gora.

**117.** Do not despise me because I am low:
people say that you are the deliverance of the fallen.

**118.** Out of your own goodness show me mercy:
I long so much to sing with my lips the glories of Gora.

**119.** I bow down to the lotus feet of Gora:
cast on me, even for a moment, a merciful glance.

**120.** Sri Narahari Dasa Thakura is my [master]:
on this alone I rely in speaking of Your glories[^23-18].

**121.** Otherwise — I, lowest of the low, sinful, wretched —
what right have I to speak of Your glories?

**122.** I am not worthy — show me mercy:
I long so much to speak of Your glories.

**123.** Come what may, I shall certainly tell it:
listen attentively to the secret of Nadia.

**124.** Knowing or not knowing, I speak with great hope:
"The Adi-khanda is ended," says this Lochana Dasa.

*Thus ends the Adi-khanda of the Sri Chaitanya-mangala, composed by Sri Lochana Dasa Thakura.*

[^23-1]: Cf. Murari 1.15–1.16. In Lochana Dasa's text the Lord meets Ishvara Puri already on the way to the Vishnupada (vv. 57–58), before the rites at the Phalgu and in Gaya itself.
[^23-2]: Gaya — a holy city in Bihar where pindas are offered to the ancestors. In performing the shraddha the Lord, as on other occasions, "instructs the people" by His own example (cf. Murari 1.15.6).
[^23-3]: V. 13+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition does not have it. Shachi asks to share in the blessing of the Gaya-shraddha performed by her son.
[^23-4]: In Murari (1.15.8) the river is called Chorandhayaka. Mandara — a holy mountain in Bihar where Madhusudana is worshipped (cf. v. 50).
[^23-5]: The Lord's companions from Bengal found fault with the local brahmanas, whose customs differed from those of Bengal; cf. Murari 1.15.12: these brahmanas had taken refuge in Madhusudana.
[^23-6]: The Lord is not subject to illness: Bhaktisiddhanta in his summary says that He "enacted the lila of being cured" in order to teach reverence for brahmanas; cf. Murari 1.15.9–13. The 1903 edition gives in its notes several more couplets from individual manuscripts (among them the Lord recalls how once in His childhood His mother gave Him the water of a brahmana's feet); A and B do not have them.
[^23-7]: Brahmanya — "kind to brahmanas", a name of the Lord. The sage Bhrigu, testing the gods, struck Vishnu on the chest with his foot, and the Lord bears the mark of his foot as a sign of respect for brahmanas (cf. Bhagavata 10.89). The 1903 edition gives vv. 45–47 only in a note as an additional reading of the printed edition.
[^23-8]: The 1983 edition: "Thus in the Padma Purana". Bhaktisiddhanta's prose rendering of the shloka (anvaya and translation) is not translated.
[^23-9]: Punahpuna (modern Punpun) — a holy river on the way to Gaya; Rajagiri (Rajagriha, modern Rajgir) with the pond Brahma-kunda — an ancient holy place in Bihar. Cf. Murari 1.15.13–14.
[^23-10]: The Lord, Himself the Teacher of the world, speaks as a seeking disciple, teaching people that everyone needs initiation from a genuine guru; according to Bhaktisiddhanta's summary He asked for the mantra in order to show mercy to Ishvara Puri. Cf. Murari 1.15.16–19.
[^23-11]: That is, the mantra of Krishna, the Lord of the gopis. In Murari (1.15.18) and in Vrindavana Dasa (Chaitanya-bhagavata, Adi 17) it is the ten-syllable mantra.
[^23-12]: After the initiation the moods of Vraja manifest in the Lord: the mood of Radha (vv. 65–67, 70), His own mood as Krishna (vv. 71–73), the mood of a servant (v. 71). Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in Gaura Krishna Himself, who has taken the mood and the radiance of Radha.
[^23-13]: Vv. 75+1 – 75+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition gives them in a note as the additional reading of another printed edition. Madhavendra Puri is Ishvara Puri's guru, the root of the tree of prema (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 9.10–11); apparently his words about Krishna's imminent appearance are meant.
[^23-14]: The Lord remembered His lila as Rama. According to the tradition of Gaya, Sita, having offered pinda to Dasharatha in Rama's absence, called the Phalgu to witness, but the river lied, and Sita cursed it to flow beneath the sand.
[^23-15]: The Pretashila, Udichi, Dakshina-manasa, Uttara-manasa, Jihvalola and the Sixteen Altars (v. 82) are holy places of Gaya where the shraddha is performed; cf. Murari 1.16.1–4.
[^23-16]: Cf. Murari 1.16.6–7. The Lord, as a devotee, laments a seeming lack of love — thus He teaches humility; at once prema awakens in Him (vv. 88–91).
[^23-17]: Cf. Murari 1.16.8–10. The heavenly voice foretells the Lord's sannyasa and His journey to Vrindavana.
[^23-18]: Narahari Sarakara Thakura of Shrikhanda is Lochana Dasa's guru. "Your" (vv. 120–122) apparently refers to Gaura (cf. v. 119).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 1 (portion 24)

*The beginning of the Madhya-khanda: the Lord teaches His students that true knowledge is devotion to Krishna; in the mood of Radha He weeps in separation, and by His word Shachi attains prema; prema in the house of Shuklambara; the Lord's associates gather; the sound of the flute and the mood of Gandharvika; the heavenly voice: "You are Ishvara Himself"; the mood of the Boar in Murari's house, the talk about the Vedas, the instruction to worship Radha and Krishna, Murari sees Rama; the explanation of the verse on the holy name*

## Prema-dana-lila (The Lord bestows prema)

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* The author, having praised the good fortune of those who were Sriman Mahaprabhu's students in His lila of teaching, describes the lila of the Lord's mercy to mother Shachi. One day, when Sriman Mahaprabhu was resting in His house, He suddenly, Himself absorbed in the mood of Srimati Radhika in separation [when Krishna had gone] to Mathura, began to weep, crying: "O Krishna, O Krishna!" Hearing the weeping, Shachi-devi came there and begged Gaurasundara for prema, and Gaurasundara bestowed prema on her. Then in the house of Shuklambara Brahmachari He enacted the lila of the great manifestation of prema. Bereft of all outward awareness, He wept all the time, crying "O Krishna!"; and hearing someone sing the name of Krishna, He fainted in bliss. Moment by moment the eight sattvika-bhavas arose all over His body, and anubhavas such as dancing, singing and rolling on the ground appeared. Gaurasundara, though Bhagavan Himself, accepted the mood of a devotee and revealed the glory of bhakti and of the devotees — therefore He is the crown jewel of all avataras.
>
> When Sriman Mahaprabhu began the lila of preaching prema, the devotees headed by Gadadhara and devotees from various lands and countries all gathered together. By Gaurahari's mercy all were maddened with prema. One day the Lord was walking with Srivasa Pandita and his brothers, and suddenly, hearing the sound of Krishna's flute, He attained the state that Srimati Gandharvika had attained and, like a madman, began to display loud laughter, weeping, silence, humility and other anubhavas. Then a heavenly voice resounded: "O Vishvambhara! You are Bhagavan Himself, descended to the earth to preach prema." When in Murari's house the Lord manifested the form of the Boar, Murari praised Him and begged the Lord for prema; the Lord told him to worship the son of the king of Vraja, served by the cowherd men and women. When Murari wished to see the form of Ramachandra, the Lord showed him that form and glorified the greatness of the name of Krishna. Further are described the coming of Brahma and the other gods to Gaura with prayers for prema, their attaining prema by Gaura's mercy and their dancing with cries of "O Radha, O Govinda!", the mercy to Shuklambara Brahmachari, the garland from the Lord's body given to Gadadhara, the wondrous beauty of the pair Gaura and Gadadhara, and so on[^24-1].

*Raga karuna-shri*

**1.** Glory to You, Lord of the life of Narahari and Gadadhara!
Be merciful, Lord — cast on us Your auspicious glance.

**2.** The Adi-khanda is ended — the beginning of the Madhya-khanda:
whoever hears it will at once obtain the treasure of prema.

**3.** I tell the story of the Madhya-khanda — the essence of nectar,
where are the pastimes in Nadia and the preaching of prema,

**4.** where the sinners Jagai and Madhai were delivered,
where prema, rare even for Brahma, was given to one and all,

**5.** where the sankirtana of the name of Hari was manifested,
where — for the deliverance of the fallen — sannyasa.

**6.** I shall tell all this — morsels of nectar:
whoever hears it, from his heart godlessness will vanish.

**7.** Having returned to Nadia, the Lord with a joyful heart
lives happily together with His relatives and friends.

**8.** All the brahmana youths living in Navadvipa
are born in good families and very pure in conduct.

**9.** Great is their fortune, they are blessed in the three worlds:
the Thakura Himself bestowed knowledge on them.

**10.** One day Gaurahari, the Lord, showing His favour,
said to all His students:

**11.** "Study the one true reality — the feet of Krishna:
that is knowledge in which there is the mark of devotion to Hari.

**12.** Without it, say the shastras, all is ignorance;
without devotion to Radha and Krishna no one is our companion.

**13.** By pride of learning, birth and wealth Krishna is not attained,
but by devotion one easily attains the Lord of the Yadus.

**14.** Consider: Krishna is subdued by the rasa of devotion" —
saying this, He recites a shloka in accordance with the shastras.

*Thus in the Padyavali (the words of a southern poet):*

**15.** "What was the conduct of the hunter, what the age of Dhruva, what the learning of Gajendra, what the name and beauty of Kubja, what the wealth of Sudama,
what the lineage of Vidura, what the prowess of Ugrasena, lord of the Yadavas? Madhava, who loves devotion, is pleased by devotion alone, not by qualities"[^24-2].

**16.** Thus the Thakura taught His students,
[intending] to reveal His prema — abundant bliss.

**17.** One day, lying in His house,
He weeps, overwhelmed by the bliss of love for Krishna.

**18.** Agitated by the mood of Radha, the Lord calls out
and in separation from [Krishna gone to] Mathura beats His breast with His hand:

**19.** "Ah, Akrura! You have carried off my Krishna!" —
saying this, the Lord weeps and laments.

**20.** "Kubja, with her base mind, has taken my Krishna —
the cheat in love, the rake, the thief of young women's hearts!"[^24-3]

**21.** Saying this, the Lord weeps and cries aloud;
His body thrills, His hair stands on end — a wondrous bhava.

**22.** Astonished, Shachi asks Vishvambhara:
"Why are You weeping, my son? What is Your sorrow?"

**23.** Hearing His mother's words, the Lord gave no answer
and weeps, beside Himself with bliss.

**24.** Then Shachi-devi, reflecting within herself,
understood by the signs: this is prema — Krishna's grace.

**25.** Great is Shachi's fortune — she knows the whole truth;
before her son she speaks sweet words:

**26.** "Listen, listen, my son, my golden son:
I see in You a wondrous [treasure], rare in the world.

**27.** Wherever You went, whatever wealth You obtained,
You brought it and gave it to me.

**28.** In Gaya You obtained such wealth — love for Krishna,
a thing rare even for the gods, a priceless jewel.

**29.** If there is any compassion for me in Your heart,
give me the wealth of love for Krishna — this I ask."

**30.** When Shachi-devi spoke such words,
the Lord's heart melted, and He looked [at her]:

**31.** "By the mercy of the Vaishnavas you will obtain prema, mother —
know it for certain: I am telling you.

**31+1.** The Vaishnava gosanis can give prema and take it away;
apart from them no one else can give prema"[^24-4].

**32.** Hearing these words, Shachi was overjoyed
and at once, unexpectedly, obtained prema-bhakti.

**33.** Her whole body thrills, her body trembles,
streams of tears flow ceaselessly from her eyes.

**34.** "Krishna, Krishna!" she calls, her heart rejoicing. —
Thus Lochana tells of Gora's first manifestation.

*Raga shri*

**35.** Then the Lord Vishvambhara, overwhelmed with love, —
[in Navadvipa] there lived a brahmana, the brahmachari Shuklambara, —

**36.** in his house the Lord weeps, lost in prema;
streams of tears flow ceaselessly from His eyes.

**37.** Mucus flows ceaselessly from His nose,
and the brahmana Shuklambara keeps wiping it away.

**38.** Rolling on the ground, He weeps day and night;
at dusk, beside Himself, He asks [the time]:

**39.** in the daytime the Lord asks: "How much of the night has passed?"
All say: "It is day now, not night."

**40.** Thus the Lord, overpowered by prema,
weeps again, helpless with bliss.

**41.** When a watch of the night has passed, He asks, [thinking it] day;
"It is not yet day," say all who are near.

**42.** Lost in prema, He does not know day from night;
hearing the name of Krishna from anyone's lips, He falls to the ground.

**43.** If anyone sings a song of Krishna's names and qualities,
He, hearing it, at once weeps, rolling on the ground.

**44.** Now He prostrates Himself and bows down,
now He sings the name of Krishna in a loud voice.

**45.** Now His voice is full of entreaty, now His body trembles,
His hair stands on end like the filaments of the kadamba.

**46.** All the time He is beside Himself and only for a moment comes to His senses —
then, at people's request, He bathes and gives gifts.

**47.** Then too He performs worship, offers food [to the Deity]
and partakes of mahaprasada.

**48.** Thus in joy and delight the day passes,
and all night long He, happy, dances and sings.

**49.** Thus in delight — day and night:
the Lord teaches the people and tastes the rasa of prema.

**50.** He Himself tastes His own rasa —
this is the chief reason; listen, all of you.

**51.** The deliverance of souls I consider a secondary reason;
that is why He is called the crown jewel of avataras[^24-5].

**52.** In His body the whole series of avataras is manifest;
the associates of all the avataras are with Him, all [His] servants.

**53.** Gaurachandra has risen in Navadvipa
and dispelled the blindness in the hearts of the people of the world.

**54.** By the rays of His compassion the age of Kali was lit up,
the burning in the hearts of all people was quenched.

**55.** All the chakora-devotees came and gathered together;
drinking the nectar of prema, all lost themselves.

**56.** Gadadhara Pandita Gosani came,
and Narahari came and remained with him.

**57.** Srinivasa, Murari, Mukunda, Vakreshvara,
Shridhara Pandita, whose home is in Navadvipa,

**58.** Shriman, Sanjaya, Pandita Dhananjaya,
Shuklambara, Nilambara and other great souls,

**59.** Sri Rama Pandita and Mahesha Pandita,
Haridasa, Nandana Acharya — the righteous,

**60.** Rudra Pandita and Pandita Damodara —
many followers of Gauranga gathered.

**61.** It is impossible to list them all by name:
the book could not be finished — it would become endless.

**62.** All the devotees who lived in various lands
came and gathered at the Lord's feet.

**63.** The devotees were maddened with great prema
and intoxicated all the people, giving them the wealth of prema.

**64.** Equally compassionate to all living beings,
Gora goes about with the devotees, delighting in prema.

**65.** And then one day Vishvambhara,
with Srivasa Pandita and his brothers —

**66.** with all of them the Lord walks along the road
and hears the sound of a flute — no one knows who is playing.

**67.** In the mood of Gandharvika, hearing the sound of the flute,
He wanders, weeping and weeping, calling and calling[^24-6].

**68.** Beside Himself, He prostrates Himself on the ground
and weeps in many ways, overflowing with prema.

**69.** The Lord is senseless, wholly in the grip of bhava;
He blesses His own and laughs aloud.

**70.** To His students He at times says unearthly things;
now He is in a frenzy, now He keeps silent.

**71.** Srivasa Pandita, Rama and Narayana,
together with Mukunda, came [with Him] to Srivasa's house.

**72.** All around are the devotees, and in the midst is Gaurahari,
intoxicated like the Youthful Couple[^24-7].

**73.** Now He rises, now falls and rolls on the ground,
cries "Hari, Hari!" and weeps aloud.

**74.** Day and night His body thrills with the bliss of prema;
there is no other talk than talk of Krishna.

**75.** Once, when He was at home, absorbed in prema,
tears flowed from His eyes in seven or five streams:

**76.** "What shall I do? Where shall I go? What means shall I find
so that My mind may somehow turn to Sri Krishna?"

**77.** Saying this, He sobs with anguished cries;
hearing His plaintive words, all the people weep.

**78.** Then a heavenly voice resounded tenderly:
"You are Ishvara Himself; listen, Vishvambhara!

**79.** You have descended to the earth to reveal prema;
by Your own mercy You will preach prema.

**80.** You will establish dharma on earth and perform kirtana;
do not grieve — perform the kirtana of Krishna.

**81.** By Your mercy in Kali the people will be delivered;
giving them Your prema, You will dispel all their sorrows.

**82.** There is no doubt in this — listen to these words:
cast off Your grief and perform Your sankirtana."

**83.** When He heard these words from the lips of the gods,
His heart rejoiced, but He did not say a word[^24-8].

**84.** On the next day — listen to a wondrous tale,
the nectar-steeped account of Vishvambhara's glories.

**85.** One day He came to the house of Murari Gupta;
His whole body thrills — signs of bhava.

**86.** He entered the shrine
and, beside Himself in bhava, began to say something.

**87.** From the oceans of His eyes flow streams of tears of love,
like the heavenly Ganga from the peak of Sumeru.

**88.** He says to all: "Look — a wonder:
before Me is a boar as large as a mountain!

**89.** At great speed he rushes on — look, the boar!
Baring his tusks, he rushes on, he wants to kill Me.

**90.** Baring his two tusks, this boar will kill Me!" —
saying this, He entered the shrine.

**91.** Then the mood of the Boar again took hold of Him:
on hands and feet He moves about the ground.

**92.** His form is round, His eyes are red,
great might, a loud roar and bellowing.

**93.** A brass vessel stood there;
raising His snout, He seized it for a moment in His teeth.

**94.** Letting go of the brass vessel and opening His mouth,
He asks Murari to declare His true nature.

**95.** In the form [of the Boar] who rescued the Vedas, Bhagavan,
the Lord, the Supreme Person, sitting down, says:

**96.** "Tell Me, what do you know of My svarupa?"
Murari says: "Lord, what do I know?"

**97.** Prostrate on the ground, Murari [said]:
"[Even] Shambhu does not know Your deeds, Lord."

**98.** Saying this, he recited a shloka of the Gita;
I shall render it in plain speech — listen, all.

*Thus in the Srimad Bhagavad-gita (10.15):*

**99.** "You alone know Yourself by Yourself, O Supreme Person,
Source of beings, Lord of beings, God of gods, Lord of the world!"

**100.** "You Yourself know Yourself, Mahaprabhu:
apart from You no one knows You."

**101.** Then Gaurahari speaks again:
"Have the Vedas the power to know Me?"

**102.** Murari again speaks plaintively:
"Even the Thousand-mouthed One does not know Your truth.

**103.** How could the Vedas know the truth of Your deeds?
No one knows Your greatness, Lord."

**104.** Hearing this, Gaura Bhagavan speaks again:
"The Vedas mock Me — listen to the account."

*Thus in the Shvetashvatara Upanishad (3.19):*

**105.** "Without hands and feet, He is swift and grasps; without eyes, He sees; without ears, He hears.
He knows all that is to be known, but none knows Him; they call Him the first, primeval Person."

**106.** "The Vedas say: 'I have no hands and feet' —
no one else mocks Me like this."

**107.** Saying this, the Lord laughs, His face serene:
"The Vedas do not know Me" — so He said[^24-9].

**108.** Then the vaidya [Murari], bowing down, said:
"Be merciful, Lord, grant me prema."

**109.** The Thakura said again: "Listen, Murari:
love Me — that is your prema.

**110.** Worship the Parabrahman in human form:
the colour of sapphire, bending in three places, a flute in His hand, —

**111.** [and Her] whose radiance humbles the pride of fresh gorochana,
by name the daughter of Vrishabhanu, the original prakriti[^24-10].

**112.** Many young beauties of Vraja give themselves
to the Beloved of the cowherd girls, the son of Nanda, [staying] by His side.

**113.** The ground [there] is chintamani, a beautiful temple of jewels,
a wish-fulfilling tree, an altar, and on it a seat.

**114.** There are kamadhenu cows of inconceivable power:
they fulfil the desire of one who meditates [on this].

**115.** The radiance of His body is called the formless Brahman.
Know: all this truth is the sweetness of Krishna"[^24-11].

**116.** Thus the Thakura spoke to all the devotees;
hearing it, the hearts of all were filled with great joy.

**117.** Hearing this, Murari says at the Lord's feet:
"Lord, [let] me see with my eyes the form of Raghunatha."

**118.** As soon as he said this, that very moment he sees:
Rama, dark as durva grass, the life of Janaki,

**119.** Lakshmana, Bharata, Shatrughna and all the rest.
Seeing this, Murari was filled with bliss.

**120.** Losing consciousness, he fell to the ground and rolls about;
the Lord, placing [on him] His lotus hand, calmed him.

**121.** He gave him a blessing: "Be full of prema:
you are Hanuman, and I am that same Ramachandra"[^24-12].

**122.** Saying this, the Lord went home.
On another day in the house of Srinivasa Pandita,

**123.** gathering all His associates,
He sat and speaks, revealing His prema.

**124.** Calling "Hari, Hari!", with delight in His heart,
He says to His own: "Listen, listen to a wonder:

**125.** how in Kali one attains Radha and Krishna —
this I shall tell you; listen with undivided attention."

**126.** Saying this, He recited a shloka from the Naradiya;
its hidden meaning people do not know.

*Thus in the Brihan-naradiya Purana (38.126):*

**127.** "The name of Hari, the name of Hari, only the name of Hari!
In Kali there is no, no, no other way."

**128.** "The Name is [the Lord's] form: the Name is the one original Person;
in Kali He is present embodied — the fool does not know this.

**129.** Know: 'only' means Bhagavan in the form of the Name;
to dispel doubt Vyasa says it three times.

**130.** Besides the three times there is one more ['only'],
to bring all ill-intentioned sinners to understanding.

**131.** By the name of Hari alone one attains kaivalya:
consider — 'kevala' means 'kaivalya'.

**132.** The plain sense of this: 'by the Name alone' is namabhasa;
its chief [fruit] is kaivalya, as the shastras proclaim.

**133.** Namabhasa gives liberation — true is the word of the shastras;
but when the Name rises [in the heart] — the bliss of prema, as the Puranas explain.

**134.** Whoever, besides this, honours another god
has no way — that is [the sense of] the threefold word.

**135.** Chant the name of Hari, meditating [on the Lord] with the cows, the gopis and the cowherd boys;
know that all this meaning is confirmed by the Vedas"[^24-13].

**136.** So spoke Gora in the mood of the Boar;
He performs sankirtana of the Name and dances, overwhelmed by prema[^24-14].

**137.** Whoever hears of Gora's glories and His pastimes in Nadia —
in him love for Krishna at once arises.

**138.** Holding a straw between his teeth, Lochana says:
I have no other wealth than the feet of Gaura.

[^24-1]: The summary covers the whole section; its end (the coming of the gods, the mercy to Shuklambara, Gadadhara) is in the next portion. Cf. Murari 2.1–2.3.
[^24-2]: A shloka from Rupa Gosvami's Padyavali. The hunter, Dhruva, Gajendra, Kubja, Sudama, Vidura, Ugrasena — devotees whom the Lord accepted for their devotion, not for their qualities. Bhaktisiddhanta's prose renderings of the shlokas (anvaya and translation) are not translated here or below.
[^24-3]: The Lord speaks in the mood of Radha separated from Krishna, whom Akrura has taken away to Mathura (cf. Bhaktisiddhanta's summary). The reproaches to Krishna ("cheat", "rake") are the language of the gopis' love in separation, not blasphemy.
[^24-4]: V. 31+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition does not have it. Cf. Murari 2.1.9–18; in Vrindavana Dasa too (Chaitanya-bhagavata, Madhya 22) Shachi obtains prema only by the mercy of a Vaishnava (Advaita).
[^24-5]: Vv. 50–51 anticipate the teaching of Krishnadasa Kaviraja (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 4): the chief reason for Gaura's appearance is the tasting of His own rasa (Radha's love), while the deliverance of souls is a secondary reason.
[^24-6]: Gandharvika — a name of Radha. Hearing the flute, the Lord experiences what Radha experienced (so in Bhaktisiddhanta's summary). Cf. Murari 2.2.1–6.
[^24-7]: Lit. "like Kishora and Kishori" — the youthful Krishna and Radha. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in Gaura both of Them in one form.
[^24-8]: Cf. Murari 2.2.7–10, where the voice from above likewise calls the Lord Bhagavan, descended to give people prema.
[^24-9]: Cf. Murari 2.2.11–24. The Upanishadic verse denies the Lord material hands and feet, but not a spiritual form: that is why He is "swift" and "grasps". The Lord jokingly calls it "mockery" when these words are taken as a denial of all form: the Vedas cannot describe Him directly (vv. 101–107). So also in Gaudiya Vaishnava theology: the Lord is a person with a spiritual body, and the impersonal Brahman is the radiance of His body (v. 115).
[^24-10]: "The original prakriti" (mula-prakriti) here means the Lord's original energy (shakti): Radha is the source of all His shaktis, not material nature.
[^24-11]: Cf. the Brahma-samhita (5.29, 5.40): Krishna's abode with ground of chintamani stones, wish-fulfilling trees and surabhi cows; the impersonal Brahman is the radiance of His body. In Bhaktisiddhanta's summary: the Lord told Murari to worship the son of the king of Vraja, served by the cowherd men and women.
[^24-12]: In the Rama lila Murari Gupta is Hanuman. Cf. Murari 2.7.13–18, where the Lord names him Ramadasa.
[^24-13]: Vv. 128–135 are an explanation of the verse "harer nama", following Murari (2.2.29–33). According to Lochana Dasa, the word "kevalam" ("only") points to kaivalya — liberation; but liberation is given already by namabhasa (the "shadow of the Name"), whereas the pure Name bestows prema (v. 133); so too Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Haridasa Thakura (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Antya 3), teach that liberation is only a by-product of namabhasa. Krishnadasa Kaviraja (Adi 17.21–25) explains differently: the threefold "name of Hari" is for emphasis, and "kevala" excludes knowledge, yoga, austerity and karma.
[^24-14]: Cf. Murari 2.2.34.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 2 (portion 25)

*The gods come to the Lord and receive prema; mercy to Shuklambara and Gadadhara; the dance in Srivasa's house — Vrindavana in Navadvipa; the Lord disperses the clouds over the kirtana; the section "Mercy to Mukunda": a song of Gaura's dance*

*Raga dhanashi*

**139.** In Navadvipa Gora is the ever-full moon:
He pours out streams of the nectar of His own prema.

**140.** The devotee-chakoras drink the nectar of His feet;
boundless compassion — prema — Gora reveals.

**141.** Hear a wonderful story of yet another day.
He sits in His own house, radiant as millions of Kamadevas:

**142.** a neck like a lion's, a throat like a conch, lotus eyes;
and aloud He speaks words deep as thunder:

**143.** "What do I see in this house — four-, five-, six-faced ones?
At the sight of them wonder grows in My heart."

**144.** Srinivasa Pandita was beside the Lord;
hearing this, he answered as the matter stood:

**145.** "All the gods have come to see You —
Brahma and the others, the four-, five- and six-faced.

**146.** You are an ocean of prema: give [them] the treasure of prema!
All the gods are begging You for the treasure of prema"[^25-1].

**147.** Then Mahaprabhu sat on a divine seat,
leaning His body on one devotee and [placing] His feet on another[^25-2].

**148.** Srinivasa and all the other devotees
fell at His feet and weep:

**149.** "I beg a boon — the honey-prema of Your lotus feet:
give it to me, Lord, O utmost limit of compassion!"

**150.** Then Lord Vishvambhara speaks in a voice like thunder:
"Take it!" — and gave to all the grace of prema.

**151.** At once prema awoke in all the gods:
their bodies, filled with bhava, became wondrous.

**152.** Crying "O Radha-Govinda!", the gods dance;
seeing it, all the Vaishnavas rejoice at heart.

**153.** The gods dance, and the goddesses with them:
tears, thrill, sweat — in waves of prema.

**154.** Now they roll on the ground, falling at His feet,
now they dance, loudly crying "Haribol!",

**155.** now they offer praise, calling "Gaura-Govinda!",
now they prostrate themselves, falling at His feet.

**156.** Now, placing His feet on their heads, the gods
beg a boon: "May my mind abide at Your feet!"

**157.** "So be it," the Lord replies, and says again and again:
"May you all be filled with the treasure of prema!"

**158.** Having received prema, the gods went to their own abodes;
seeing it, all the devotees rejoiced at heart.

**159.** Such mercy did the Lover of His devotees show.
Seeing this display of mercy, Shuklambara speaks.

**160.** Shuklambara Brahmachari is most pure:
his body is hallowed by holy places, his nature is gentle.

**161.** Before the Lord he speaks without fear;
longing for prema, he says all that is in his heart:

**162.** "Hear, hear, O Lord, Gaura Bhagavan:
at last today my eyes have found their joy.

**163.** I have travelled to many holy places,
[suffered] many torments and hardships — and have understood nothing.

**164.** I have travelled to Madhupuri and Dvaravati,
and still I am unhappy: give me the treasure of prema!"

**165.** Hearing these words, the Lord answered:
"Hear one word of Mine, Shuklambara:

**166.** in those forests live ever so many jackals and dogs —
what is that to Me?" said the Thakura[^25-3].

**167.** "As long as Krishna does not rise in the heart,
so long one has no grace from the holy places.

**168.** Without love for Krishna any dharma is nothing;
read and see: the shastras all say this."

*Thus [it is said]:*

**169.** "The fish is always bathing, the snake lives on air, the ram eats leaves, the oil-presser's ox is ever going round, the heron always stands in meditation,
the mouse lives in a hole, the lion always dwells in the thicket — but alas! what fruit do they have from austerity without perfection in true feeling?"[^25-4]

*Thus in the Narada-pancharatra (1.2.6):*

**170.** "If Hari is worshipped, what need of austerity? If Hari is not worshipped, what use is austerity?
If Hari is within and without, what need of austerity? If Hari is not within and without, what use is austerity?"

**171.** Hearing these words, the brahmana fell to the ground;
distraught, he weeps, and his longing grows.

**172.** The Lord cannot bear the distress of one devoted to Him:
Gaura's body flushed red with compassion.

**173.** "I have given prema, given prema!" He cries aloud,
and the brahmana Shuklambara obtained the grace of prema.

**174.** At once he obtained prema: his body trembles,
his whole body thrills, there are tears in his eyes.

**174+1.** Rejoicing, he repeats the name of Krishna;
the whole night passed in the rasa of Krishna[^25-5].

**175.** Rejoicing, he performs sankirtana of [the Lord's] qualities and names;
seeing it, all the people were very glad.

**176.** Pandita Sri Gadadhara — the abode of all virtues —
stays constantly with the Lord and repeats the Name.

**177.** One night he slept beside the Lord;
seeing his longing, the Lord, pleased, said:

**178.** "You will obtain rare prema at the dawn of this night:
by the grace of the Vaishnavas your desire will be fulfilled"[^25-6].

**179.** Saying this, He put the garland from His own body round his neck.
In the morning all came to see the Lord.

**180.** The Lord told everyone what had happened in the night —
and in the course of that talk Gadadhara Pandita obtained prema.

**181.** With great joy at heart he bathed in the waters of the Ganga;
his body, helpless with prema, sways.

**182.** He performed the worship of Jagannatha-deva according to the rules,
and worships again — before his own Lord:

**183.** he anointed His body with fragrant sandal paste,
put a divine garland round His neck and offers praise.

**184.** So every day he serves [the Lord]:
in the bedchamber he prepares His bed

**185.** and every night sleeps at His feet;
his heart is always full of faith and devotion[^25-7].

**186.** Before the Lord he speaks nectarean words;
hearing them, Lord Vishvambhara rejoices at heart.

**187.** His nectarean speech sprinkled [the Lord's] heart:
the Lord, taking him by the hand, goes to dance.

**188.** Placing His other arm on Narahari's arm,
the Charmer of the rasa dances in Srivasa's house.

**189.** In Gaura's body the devotees see a dark form,
and Gadadhara then took the form of Radha.

**190.** Narahari at that moment became Madhumati;
seeing this, all the Vaishnavas cry "Hari, Hari!"

**191.** In that place Vrindavana appeared:
the son of Shachi — with the cows, the gopis and the cowherd boys.

**192.** As the friends and girl-friends had been before,
so the devotees became beside the Lord, tasting rasa.

**193.** Sri Raghunandana is a new Kamadeva:
he is called the transcendental Madana.

**194.** All of them, taking their former bodies, beside the Lord
dance round the Lord, circle within circle.

**195.** Seeing this, all the associates of other avataras weep:
in Navadvipa the Moon of Vraja has risen!

**196.** Now it is Gaura's lila with Gadadhara,
now Shyama's lila, the joy of the rasa of the rasa dance with Radha[^25-8].

**197.** Seeing the wondrous lila, all the devotees
cry again and again: "Hari, Hari! Jaya, jaya!"

**198.** The day ended; it was evening, the quarters were lovely —
and suddenly clouds covered the vault of the sky.

**199.** Again and again the thunder rolls;
seeing this, the Vaishnavas took it for a calamity.

**200.** Seeing that a hindrance had come, all were dejected;
anxiously they think: "How can the hindrance be removed?"

**201.** [But] the clouds had come to receive the grace of prema:
seeing Gaura's lila, they thundered with love.

**202.** Then Mahaprabhu took the mandira cymbals in His hands
and loudly sings the sankirtana of names and qualities.

**203.** Wishing to fulfil [the desires of] the heavenly world,
the Lord raised His face and gazes at the sky.

**204.** The clouds went far away, the sky cleared,
and the jubilation of the Vaishnavas increased.

**205.** The night became clear, tinged by the moon;
the devotees sing His qualities, and He Himself dances.

**206.** The clouds, taking their own form, beside the Lord
dance, following the devotees.

**207.** In giving this prema Gaurahari makes no distinction:
what to speak of clouds — He filled the three worlds with it!

**208.** The Thakura Himself dances with the devotees:
amid the ecstasy of all dances the son of Shachi.

**209.** In the ecstasy of prema the great King of dancers dances;
on His lotus feet the anklets ring loudly.

**210.** The virtuous wives of the brahmanas joyfully cry "Jaya, jaya!";
from the sky the gods watch the festival[^25-9].

**211.** All the devotees dance, intoxicated with prema:
who knows what austerities they performed — for how many births —

**212.** that for them they dance with the Thakura
and make merry with such a treasure as prema?

**213.** With His compassion the Lord covered both earth and sky.
Hearing of it, Lochana Dasa speaks in joy.

## Mercy to Mukunda

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* The author, having described the wondrous beauty and loveliness of Sriman Mahaprabhu, glorifies the lila of planting the mango tree. One day Sriman Mahaprabhu, before the devotees, planted a mango stone; before their eyes it at once sprouted, gradually became a tree and blossomed; mangoes appeared on the tree and at once ripened; the devotees offered them to Bhagavan and all together honoured the prasada. Afterwards they saw that none of it was there any more — everything had vanished. Then Sriman Mahaprabhu, by the example of that tree, showed the illusoriness of samsara and taught how to conquer maya; He instructed Mukunda Datta in the worship of Sri Krishna, whose form is full of sweetness, and bade him give up speculation about the Self (adhyatma) and serve Bhagavan. When Murari Gupta asked Sriman Mahaprabhu for the mercy of bhakti, the Lord blessed him. Srivasa and Sri Rama Pandita are very dear to Sriman Mahaprabhu; in their house Sriman Mahaprabhu enjoyed the bliss of kirtana with the devotees. One day, when a certain foolish brahmana called Sri Krishna a product of maya, Sriman Mahaprabhu, hearing it, bathed in the Ganga with His clothes on[^25-10].

*Raga shyamagada. [Opening:]*
*Like the peak of Sumeru is His beautiful tall body;
under the weight of prema He sways.
His whole body thrills from feet to crown,
His red eyes brim with tears.*

*Refrain: "The town of Nadia rejoices: splendidly dances Shachi's son!"*

**1.** The four brothers — Srinivasa and his brothers — joyfully sing auspicious songs,
Haridasa cries "Hari, Hari!"
[Gora is] like Kishori and Kishora; [all] loudly proclaim Gora's glories,
roaring "hum!" in the surge of prema.

**2.** Murari and Mukunda Datta sing His qualities without pause,
exultant, thrilling.
In hope of the nectar of prema, near His lotus feet
they hover like intoxicated bees.

**3.** All around they cry "Jaya!", and in the middle dances golden Gaura;
everyone is beside himself with bliss.
Wherever one looks, there is joy everywhere,
in all ten directions — the weeping of prema.

**4.** Some, meeting in twos, embrace in the bliss of prema,
some sing His fame like bards.
Falling at His feet, Pandita Gosani says:
"You have spread out a wondrous market!"[^25-11]

**5.** As if golden pearls were strung — so the thrill [covers] His body,
His face is red with love.
In the languor of rasa He laughs, languid in rapture,
and reveals the treasure of His heart.

**6.** Now He speaks unearthly things, as if intoxicated,
now He says: "I am Bhagavan!"
Now He bows down, now He pronounces blessings,
now He grants boons to His own.

**7.** The Lord reveals a prema never seen before:
a trembling seized the seven continents.
All — women and men — seeing what Gora experienced, [forgot themselves],
and in prema this Lochana Dasa forgot himself.

*Raga dhanashi, tune taraja; refrain: "What creator is this — he made beautiful Gauranga and kept his own body? How hard he is, with a heart of wood and stone: seeing this beauty, he did not melt away!"*[^25-12]

**8.** Who, churning nectar, took off its butter
and from it fashioned Gora's body?
Who sifted the whole world, squeezed out its rasa
and blended it into one — pure tenderness?

**9.** Who set the curd of anuraga with a culture of prema
and made [of it] His two eyes?
Sweeter still are His soft words:
smiling, He lets them fall one by one.

**10.** Who boiled down an unbroken stream of nectar
till it became sugar the colour of gold?
Who, beating that sugar, raised the froth? —
so Gora's body seems to me.

**11.** Who, grinding lightning, rubbed His body with it,
rubbed His face with the moon?
Who, grinding loveliness, painted this picture —
the wondrous fashioning of [His] form?

**12.** All the full moons weep, distraught,
at the fragrance of the lotuses of His hands and feet.
The radiance of His twenty nails lit up the world:
those blind from birth received sight.

**13.** Such a charming Gora is seen nowhere —
in the wondrous play of prema.
Men, in the mood of women, weep, distraught —
how can a woman keep life [in her body]?

**14.** Who made His heart — an abode of play — from the rasa of all rasas,
shaping it with delight?
Who, grinding Madana, fashioned His face?
And I, without Him, must die in tears.

**15.** Who brought Indra's bow
and drew with it the line of sandal on Gora's forehead?
At the sight of this form all the women of noble families
long to turn their arms into wings.

**16.** [His body,] a temple of delight, was carved
with great care from many jewels.
The art of playful lilas, the play of bhavas...
thinking of Madana's pain, [a woman] weeps.

**17.** He does not glance from the corners of His eyes, yet always in everyone's heart
the eye-birds fly to see Him.
Seeing the eyes' thirst, the lips too are filled with longing;
the body is languid and exhausted.

**18.** Chaste wives leave their families, the lame run headlong,
asuras and atheists sing His qualities,
roll in the dust and weep; no one can stay composed:
Gora's qualities are unending nectar.

**19.** "Run, run!" they cry, and embrace in the bliss of prema;
some dance, some laugh aloud.
Even the modest wife of a noble house says: "Let everything go!" —
in the breeze of Gora's qualities and beauty.

**20.** The wives of the town of Nadia, beholding the moon of Gora's face,
ever shed streams of tears.
Their breasts fill with love, their bodies thrill,
in their hearts [He] is ever awake.

**21.** Even the best of yogis and sages, pondering day and night,
are bewildered by Gora's qualities.
The Lord of all worlds rolls in the dust and weeps,
ever remembering: "Radha, Radha!"

**22.** Leaving His play with Lakshmi, He longs for prema;
both His eyes are red with love.
[His] heart, absorbed in meditation on Radha, is not revealed;
the witness to it is that golden body[^25-13].

**23.** Look, look, people — what wondrous prema!
Though He is the Lord of the lords of the three worlds,
in the company of the destitute — who knows what wealth He begs? —
with what joy He goes about dancing!

**24.** Glory, glory, glory! Such a storehouse of the rasa of prema
Gora-raya broke open and gave away.
The lifeless received life, the lame leapt over mountains —
joyfully sings Lochana Dasa.

[^25-1]: The four-faced one is Brahma, the five-faced Shiva, the six-faced Skanda (Karttikeya). Cf. Murari 2.3.1–2.
[^25-2]: Cf. Murari 2.3.3–4: the Lord touches the devotees' bodies with His feet, and they beg devotion to His feet.
[^25-3]: Cf. Murari 2.3.5–8. The Lord does not belittle Mathura and Dvaraka: He says that mere bodily residence in holy places, without love for Krishna, does not yet bear fruit (cf. Bhagavata 10.84.13).
[^25-4]: "True feeling" (sad-bhava) is explained by Bhaktisiddhanta in his gloss on the verse as purity of mind. The source of the verse is not given in the edition.
[^25-5]: V. 174+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition; the 1903 edition lacks it too).
[^25-6]: Cf. Murari 2.3.10–17; there the Lord tells Gadadhara that in the morning he will distribute His prasada to the Vaishnavas.
[^25-7]: According to Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition, Gadadhara Pandita is Sri Radha Herself (Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika 147–153); Murari (2.3.17) compares this service to Radha preparing Krishna's bed.
[^25-8]: Vv. 188–196: Vrindavana and the rasa-lila appear in Srivasa's house; Gadadhara is Radha, Narahari Madhumati (see the Sutra-khanda, en/08, v. 645), Raghunandana "the transcendental (aprakrita) Madana", i.e. Kandarpa, not the mundane god of lust (in the Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika — Pradyumna; cf. en/08, note 19). The rasa here is a spiritual lila, not mundane love. "Circle within circle" — lit. "in the order of the enclosures (avarana)": the associates surround the Lord in circles, as in meditation on His abode. Lochana Dasa, a disciple of Narahari, brings the Shrikhanda devotees into this scene; other biographies do not describe it.
[^25-9]: Vv. 198–213 — cf. Murari 2.3.18–27. The brahmanas' wives raise the auspicious cry "ulu-ulu".
[^25-10]: The summary covers the whole section; this portion contains only the song of Gaura's dance (vv. 1–24); the rest (the mango tree, the instructions to Mukunda and Murari, Srivasa and Sri Rama, the blaspheming brahmana) is in the next portion.
[^25-11]: Pandita Gosani is Gadadhara Pandita. The "market" is an image of the lavish distribution of prema.
[^25-12]: The voices of this song are the women of Nadia (vv. 13–20): here again is Lochana Dasa's "nagari-bhava" (see en/09, note 9). The "creator" who supposedly made Gaura is a poetic figure: the Lord's form is eternal and created by no one.
[^25-13]: That is, the Lord hides that His heart is absorbed in Radha, but His golden complexion — Radha's complexion — betrays it. So too in Gaudiya Vaishnava theology: Gaura is Krishna who has taken Radha's bhava and complexion (cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 4).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 3 (portion 26)

*The mango tree — a lesson on maya and on offering all acts to Krishna; instruction to Mukunda on worshipping two-armed Krishna; Murari begs for mercy; Srivasa and Sri Rama; the blaspheming brahmana and the Lord's bath; the section "The Truth about Advaita": the Lord with Advaita Acharya*

*Raga varadi; refrain: "Hari, Rama, Narayana — Gora, Shachi's darling!"*

**25.** Hear yet another wonderful story of another day:
every day the son of Shachi reveals something new.

**26.** This story is most unusual, unknown to people;
in the hearts of low people faith in it does not arise.

**27.** Only in secret does He reveal His lordship;
to His own He says: "See: this world is illusory"[^26-1].

**28.** Saying this, He spoke of something else;
all forgot [about it] and repeat the name of Hari.

**29.** [His] heart became intoxicated with the sankirtana of His own name;
overpowered by prema, He rolls on the ground and weeps.

**30.** Suddenly He rises and speaks, clapping His hands —
He reveals His lordship to His own:

**31.** "Look, look: I have planted a mango stone —
the tree I have raised will grow by itself!"

**32.** Then all suddenly say:
"He has only just planted the stone — and it has sprouted already!"

**33.** Before their eyes the tree put out shoots,
fine branches grew, the tree was covered with buds.

**34.** "Look, look, all you people — another wonder:
see, My little tree is covered with buds!"

**35.** At once fruits appeared and at that very moment ripened;
pointing with His finger, the Lord shows them to all.

**36.** They plucked the fruits and brought them — all the people see it —
and, making an offering, placed them before the Ishvara.

**37.** In a moment none of it is to be seen:
only the fruits remain, and the tree has proved illusory.

**38.** Such maya He showed, and He tells all the people:
"Knowing this, do not die of the grief of this world.

**39.** By the power of My maya the whole world is created;
not understanding this, all people call it their own.

**40.** Who can tear the rope of My maya?
There is only one way to conquer maya[^26-2]:

**41.** if all the acts that people perform as duties of the body,
all their acts, people dedicate to Me,

**42.** then the body is given to Krishna's feet,
and there is no more bondage of action or inaction, of good or evil.

**43.** This bhakti is the highest truth: reckon it as dedication;
when one gives [all] to Krishna, separateness of itself does not remain[^26-3].

**44.** Having dedicated all, one surely attains Krishna —
so sing all the Puranas, the Gita and the Bhagavata.

**45.** Otherwise all this is fruitless;
if one dedicates everything to the Ishvara, life in the world becomes meaningful"[^26-4].

**46.** Such is the wondrous manifestation of Gorachand.
Hearing of it, Lochana Dasa speaks in joy.

*Raga shri; refrain: "O-ki ho-re, Gauranga, jaya, jaya!"*

**47.** At that time, seeing the vaidya Mukunda,
Mahaprabhu spoke with a slight smile:

**48.** "You, they say, honour the knowledge of Brahman — so I have heard.
Well, Mukunda Datta, I praise you!"

**49.** Saying this, the Thakura recited this shloka;
hearing it, everyone's heart trembled.

*Thus in the Padma-purana (quoted in Kavi-karnapura's Chaitanya-charitamrita, 6.36):*

**50.** "The yogis delight in the Infinite — in the Truth, the Bliss, the One whose self is consciousness;
therefore the Supreme Brahman is called by the word 'Rama'"[^26-5].

**51.** Then Bhagavan Gaurahari
again, showing favour, said to the vaidya:

**52.** "You think highly of worshipping the Four-armed,
but in meditation on the Two-armed your knowledge is small.

**53.** If you want all riches and your own good,
worship two-armed Krishna, immersing your heart in Him.

**54.** Narayana is a manifestation of Krishna, say the shastras;
there is no word that Krishna comes from Narayana"[^26-6].

**55.** Such compassionate words Vishvambhara speaks;
hearing them, the vaidya respectfully bowed his head:

**56.** "I have bathed my fill in the waters of the heavenly river;
the dust of the Vaishnavas' feet is [for me] the highest grace.

**57.** Let Your lotus feet be an umbrella over my head:
perform my anointing as a servant — that alone I ask.

**58.** What do I know, Lord, of my own good and ill?
Always, within and without, I am blinded by pride.

**59.** When by Your own goodness You show me mercy,
then grant me the grace of serving You.

**60.** You are the Lord of the lords of all, the embodiment of bliss;
You are that very son of Nanda, the root of the avataras."

**61.** Hearing these words, the Lord, pleased at heart,
touched his head with His lotus foot.

**62.** [Mukunda's] whole body thrilled, his eyes filled with tears,
the vaidya's speech faltered — signs of prema.

**63.** In a faltering voice he offered long praise:
"Glory to the Great Maheshvara, the One beyond all causes!"[^26-7]

**64.** Then Mahaprabhu Vishvambhara Hari,
seeing Murari, began to speak:

**65.** "Hear, hear, vaidya, My word:
give up [your] song — the speculation about the Self that occupies your mind[^26-8].

**66.** If you have the desire to live,
and if you still have the wish [to taste] the bliss of love for Krishna,

**67.** then give up speculation about the Self
and sing the sankirtana of His qualities with love for Krishna.

**68.** The crown of the best of dancers, beautiful, dark of body,
radiant as a sapphire, with a choice flute in His hand,

**69.** in yellow garments, with a forest garland on His neck —
yet that Lord, surrounded by the gopis, you do not worship!"

**70.** Hearing the Lord's commanding words, Murari the vaidya
fell to the ground in distress and weeps.

**71.** At the Lord's feet he humbly pleads at length:
"Lord, I cannot cross the impassable samsara.

**72.** Neither Brahma nor Maheshvara, nor Lakshmi nor Ananta
can conquer maya — so invincible is she[^26-9].

**73.** Who can conquer the all-powerful maya
without Your grace? — hear, Vishvambhara.

**74.** I am the lowest of the low; what power have I
to conquer samsara and serve Your feet?

**75.** If, seeing me wretched, You show me mercy,
[then], Lord, embodiment of compassion, I shall serve You.

**76.** For so long the treasure of prema was hidden —
[and now] You, Lord, have revealed it out of compassion.

**77.** Let my mind, like a bee,
drink the nectar-prema of Your lotus feet.

**78.** Give me this boon, ocean of compassion;
do not despise me — I am most wretched."

**79.** Hearing such humble words, the Thakura
was filled with compassion and great joy.

**80.** Smiling, the Lord said: "Hear, Murari:
soon your desire will be fulfilled"[^26-10].

**81.** And Srinivasa Pandita Thakura
is a devotee of the purest mind and very wise.

**82.** Every day he serves Krishna together with his brothers;
with all his heart he worships Vishvambhara's feet.

**83.** Every day he performs sankirtana of names and qualities;
with his younger brother Rama he has great love.

**84.** Sri Rama Pandita is devoted to serving his elder brother;
the two brothers together sing songs of Hari's qualities.

**85.** Srivasa and Sri Rama are two favourites of the Lord;
with them He plays with a joyful heart.

**86.** In their house the Lord dances with them all,
like Kapila Thakura in the circle of sages[^26-11].

**87.** Thus in joy and merriment the days pass;
hundreds of students He Himself teaches.

**88.** The students reason among themselves;
among them was one great ignoramus.

**89.** "The one they call Sri Krishna is but maya" —
so said the foolish son of a brahmana.

**90.** Hearing this, the Thakura covered His ears with His hands
and at once went to bathe in the heavenly river.

**91.** In His clothes, together with the students, He bathes in the Ganga,
thrilling, repeating the name of Hari again and again:

**92.** "Sinful, low, wretched, godless in character —
with his evil words he has defiled My ears!"[^26-12]

**93.** Saying this, He repeats the name of Hari again and again.
Lochana says: Gora is the abode of all virtues.

## The Truth about Advaita

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One day Sriman Mahaprabhu [with the devotees] came to the house of Sri Advaita Prabhu to see Him; Advaita Prabhu fell at the feet of Sriman Mahaprabhu. Mahaprabhu lovingly embraced Advaita Prabhu and in conversation glorified the pre-eminence of prema-bhakti in the age of Kali. Seeing a certain godless brahmana, Srivasa Pandita grew alarmed, and by the will of Sriman Mahaprabhu that brahmana was bewildered by maya. Then [the Lord], having enjoyed kirtana and a meal in Advaita's house, returned home. Afterwards Sriman Mahaprabhu, [having expounded the doctrine of the Self,] spoke of the greatness of supreme bhakti.
>
> Sri Advaita Acharya Prabhu came to Navadvipa and prostrated Himself fully at the feet of Sriman Mahaprabhu. At that time Sriman Mahaprabhu, in Srivasa's house, was worshipping His weapon, the club, in order to destroy the godless. Sriman Mahaprabhu, [greatly pleased] with Acharya Gosani, declared before the people, "Bhagavan descends by Advaita's desire," mounted a couch and ordered Advaita Prabhu to dance and sing kirtana. When Srivasa Pandita asked about the truth of Advaita, Sriman Mahaprabhu told the truth about Advaita and instructed everyone to serve Bhagavan[^26-13].

*Raga bhatiyari; refrain: "Hari, Narayana, Gorachand, Shachi's darling — with the noose of prema He has bound the hearts of living beings!"*

**1.** Now I shall tell another wondrous story:
listen, all, attentively, setting other thoughts aside.

**2.** When one tells of Gora's qualities, the body thrills:
such is the power of the unending nectar of Gora's qualities.

**3.** Together with Srinivasa and the other disciples
He set out joyfully to see Advaita Acharya[^26-14].

**4.** Some sing songs, some repeat the name of Hari,
they cry "Hari, Hari!" — beyond compare.

**5.** The Thakura Himself dances, the devotees sing;
by the power of prema they do not know themselves.

**6.** From head to foot [all] thrill, their eyes are red;
gazing at Gora's face, they sway.

**7.** The Lord slaps His arms like a wrestler and roars "hum!";
rolling on the ground, all the associates weep.

**8.** Thus in joy they go along the road,
longing to see Advaita Acharya Gosani.

**9.** Arriving, He saw Advaita Acharya Gosani
and, falling to the ground, prostrated Himself.

**10.** In dismay Acharya Gosani fell at His feet
and with much humility speaks piteously,

**11.** bowing and rolling on the ground:
"[You are] the crown of millions of Advaitas like me!"[^26-15]

**12.** They embrace each other
and bathe each other with the tears of their eyes.
