**13.** Sitting down, the Lord speaks His words —
captivating, sin-removing, bestowing prema-bhakti.

**14.** Having heard, Acharya Gosani speaks;
His eyes redden as He rebukes the godless:

**15.** "The godless say, 'In the Kali-yuga there is no bhakti' —
let them see with their own eyes my Chaitanya Gosani!"

**16.** Hearing these words, the Lord, His lips trembling,
began to reply in a voice deep as thunder:

**17.** "'There is no bhakti in the Kali-yuga' — what else is there, then?
There is bhakti alone — by it the world lives.

**18.** One who says, 'There is no bhakti in the Kali-yuga' —
his birth is in vain; hear, all of you.

**19.** In the Kali-yuga there is devotion to Krishna, [given] by [the Lord's] gracious love:
in no other yuga is there such mercy as in Kali"[^26-16].

**20.** At this time Pandita Srivasa,
with fear in his heart, began to speak:

**21.** "See, Lord, there in front — a godless brahmana:
now he will disrupt Krishna's festival.

**22.** He is a great godless man, of very wicked conduct;
proud of his learning, he is exceedingly arrogant."

**23.** Then Mahaprabhu said to him:
"This wicked scoundrel will not come here."

**24.** And the brahmana, bewildered by maya, did not come[^26-17],
while Mahaprabhu, joyful at heart, gives Himself to play:

**25.** placing one arm on Srinivasa's arm,
with His left hand He holds Gadadhara's hand;

**26.** leaning His body on Narahari,
He gazes at Sri Raghunandana's face and weeps;

**27.** placing His lotus feet on Sri Rama Pandita,
Mahaprabhu plays before the Acharya.

**28.** All around the Vaishnavas sing the sankirtana of His qualities,
and in the middle dances the Lord, the son of Shachi,

**29.** as at the festival of the rasa, surrounded by the gopis —
so beautiful was [His play] in the midst of the kirtana.

**30.** Thus after a while, when the dance was over,
Advaita Acharya, together with Sita, rejoiced.

**31.** Then in His house the Lord took His meal;
His body was anointed with fragrant sandal paste and adorned with a garland.

**32.** Advaita Acharya considered Himself blessed:
"Now I have known the Lord's mercy to me!"

**33.** Advaita's people weep, fallen at His feet;
Vishvambhara lifts them all up and embraces them.

**34.** Dancing and singing His own names and qualities,
the Lord returned home with His own.

**35.** Thus day by day [His] manifestation grows.
Hearing of it, Lochana Dasa rejoices at heart.

[^26-1]: "Illusory" (michha) in the sense of transient and deceptive: the world is created by the Lord's energy and is real, but it is false to regard it as one's own and to seek happiness in it (v. 39). Cf. Murari 2.4.6–11, where the same miracle with the tree is called "a conjurer's trick".
[^26-2]: Cf. Bhagavad-gita 7.14: the Lord's maya is hard to overcome, but those who surrender to Him overcome it. Here maya is the Lord's external energy, which He controls.
[^26-3]: Not merging with Krishna: one who has given everything to Krishna has no interests left apart from His will (cf. Bhagavata 7.5.23 on atma-nivedana, and 11.29.34).
[^26-4]: Cf. Murari 2.4.10–11: everything done for the Master bears fruit.
[^26-5]: A verse of the Padma-purana; it is quoted by Murari (2.4.14) and in the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 9.29). The Lord jokingly "praises" Mukunda for his knowledge of Brahman and shows that the Supreme Brahman is Rama Himself, a Person.
[^26-6]: So also in Gaudiya Vaishnava theology: Krishna is the original Personality of Godhead Himself ("krishnas tu bhagavan svayam", Bhagavata 1.3.28), and Narayana is His manifestation. Cf. Murari 2.4.15–17.
[^26-7]: Cf. Murari 2.4.18–21.
[^26-8]: Lit. "give up the gita (song)…". In Murari (2.4.22–23) the Lord reproaches Murari for his song on the doctrine of the Self and tells him to compose a shloka about Hari. The vaidya is Murari Gupta, who like Mukunda was of the vaidya community.
[^26-9]: Murari's humble exaggeration: in Gaudiya Vaishnava theology Lakshmi is the Lord's internal energy and Ananta His expansion, and maya has no power over them. The point is that no one can conquer maya by his own strength (cf. Gita 7.14).
[^26-10]: Cf. Murari 2.4.22–27; there the Lord's blessing is understood as permission for Murari to tell of His descent.
[^26-11]: Cf. Murari 2.4.27–32. Kapila is the sage, a descent of Vishnu, teacher of sankhya.
[^26-12]: Cf. Murari 2.4.33–34. The words "Krishna is maya" express the Mayavada view, in which Krishna's form is a product of maya; for Vaishnavas this is the gravest offence: Krishna's form is eternal and spiritual (cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 17.129–133).
[^26-13]: The summary covers the whole section; this portion contains vv. 1–35 (the Lord at Advaita's), the rest is in the next portion. Some places in the summary are illegible in the OCR of the edition and are restored by sense (in square brackets).
[^26-14]: Cf. Murari 2.5.1–19: the Lord goes to Advaita in Shantipur.
[^26-15]: Cf. Murari 2.5.3–5: the Lord first prostrates Himself before Advaita, "honouring the Vaishnava as Vishnu Himself", and Advaita in dismay falls at His feet. In Gaudiya Vaishnava theology Advaita is Maha-Vishnu and the Lord's eternal servant; His humility is an example for devotees.
[^26-16]: "Gracious love" — in the original "prasanna maya": the Bengali word maya is used here in its ordinary sense of "tenderness, love, compassion", not illusion. Cf. Murari 2.5.7–10.
[^26-17]: Cf. Murari 2.5.11–13: "blinded by the maya of Vishnu". Maya here is the Lord's external energy, which by His will keeps non-devotees away from the kirtana.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 4 (portion 27)

*The Lord expounds the path of knowledge and proclaims what is highest — bhakti; meditation on Radha and Krishna in Vrindavana; Advaita comes to Navadvipa; the Lord with His club threatens the haters of devotees; Advaita's dance; the truth about Advaita; the ban on speculating about the Self; a song of the beauty of dancing Gaura*

*Raga varadi; refrain: "I take upon myself all misfortunes of Gora's beauty! He has given away the treasure of prema, filling the world with it"*

**36.** Then Mahaprabhu, sitting in His own house,
the Ishvara, speaks of the truth of the Self (adhyatma-tattva):

**37.** "Krishna alone is the Master, the creation and maintenance [of the world];
He Himself, as the one Soul, abides [in all] on earth."

**38.** Saying this, He opened His hand and again closed it into a fist,
showing everyone: "Such is My creation."

**39.** And again He says: "The Truth is by nature pure being;
hear, all of you, what absorption in it is.

**40.** And yet people strive for that true being:
without knowledge of the One there is no liberation — that is why.

**41.** [Without it] one cannot conquer the grievous bonds of samsara;
if one gains knowledge of the One, the bonds fall away.

**42.** Without liberation there is never knowledge of Krishna;
therefore I say: hear — the Lord is reached through knowledge[^27-1].

**43.** Here, look at these five fingers of My hand:
one is smeared with honey, four with something repulsive.

**44.** Because of the stench one does not even want to look at them,
but the finger with honey one carefully licks with the tongue.

**45.** The One, the Imperishable is Bhagavan alone;
apart from Him there is nothing by which to be liberated."

**46.** Thus in many ways He set forth the path of knowledge (jnana-yoga)
and for a moment fell silent — the Ocean of virtues.

**47.** Then, out of mercy, He again speaks the essence of all truths:
"Without devotion to Sri Krishna there is nothing else."

**48.** He explained to all: "Krishna is reached through knowledge,
[but] prema-bhakti for Krishna's lotus feet is the essence of all.

**49.** When this knowledge comes, the mind turns firmly to Krishna;
when the mind is firm, causeless bhakti arises"[^27-2].

**50.** Then He became absorbed in meditation on Krishna's lotus feet
and, crying "Hari, Hari!", remembered His lotus feet:

**51.** together with Radha — dark, thrice-bent, [the embodiment of] consciousness and bliss,
the enchanter of Kamadeva, the best of dancers, of many sports,

**52.** in Vrindavana, in a new temple of jewels,
captivating, surrounded by the beautiful cowherd girls.

**53.** Cuckoos, peacocks, sarikas, parrots, swarms of bees;
Vrindavana in full bloom is adorned with many flowers.

**54.** The ground is chintamani, everywhere wish-fulfilling trees,
the kamadhenu cows like herds of surabhis.

**55.** Girdled by the Yamuna, captivating, full of beauty —
seeing that rasa and loveliness, Lakshmi is filled with longing[^27-3].

**56.** A stream of prema rose and flows from both His eyes,
His body thrills, His eyes are red.

**57.** Now He laughs, now weeps, now dances and sings;
in a faltering voice the Lord said to all:

**58.** "Such are all My devotees:
by their virtues they purify the three worlds."

**59.** Saying this, the Lord, joyful, with His devotees
makes them all dance and dances Himself.

**60.** Thus He lives happily in Navadvipa
together with His devotees, by the Ganga.

**61.** And then on another day Advaita Acharya Gosani
came to Navadvipa to see Vishvambhara.

**62.** Mahaprabhu had gone to Srinivasa's house;
the Acharya, about to come [to Him], performs His bath and worship.

**63.** In Srinivasa's house the Lord, with a joyful heart,
placing a flower on the tip of a staff, said:

**64.** "I have worshipped the club to destroy these wicked ones —
all who do harm to My devotees.

**65.** With it I shall destroy all such people" —
so the Lord spoke in the presence of all.

**66.** "There is one wicked man, a hater of My devotees:
he will suffer leprosy for many births.

**67.** I shall make him dwell in the hell of the pishachas;
he himself will become a hog that eats filth.

**68.** I shall punish his disciples too;
with My club I shall destroy all the godless[^27-4].

**69.** I had thought to go to the forest —
but here for Me is that great forest:

**70.** some are like tigers, others like stones,
some are like trees, others like grass,

**71.** and some I count as cattle —
that is why I say: this is a great forest for Me[^27-5].

**72.** I have heard that Advaita Acharya has come here;
if He does not come here, I shall go there Myself."

**73.** At that very moment the Acharya suddenly came
and appeared before the Lord.

**74.** Placing gifts near the lotus feet,
He fell to the ground and prostrated Himself.

**75.** Taking His hand, the Lord says:
"I have come here for Your sake.

**76.** You held My lotus feet on Your head
and, weeping, worshipped them, offering tulasi buds.

**77.** You, whose heart is that of a bhagavata, brought [Me] with Your loud calls:
thanks to Your love all have obtained Me"[^27-6].

**78.** Saying this, Mahaprabhu sat on a couch
and ordered the Acharya: "Dance!"

**79.** Then Advaita Acharya, the best of brahmanas,
danced long to songs of the ten avataras.

**80.** Srivasa Pandita and all the other devotees,
beside themselves with joy, sing the sankirtana of His qualities.

**81.** Seeing this, Mahaprabhu Gaura Bhagavan,
pleased, said to Him with a serene face:

**82.** "All these children of Yours beg Me for prema;
I shall give them the gift of prema-bhakti — I tell You"[^27-7].

**83.** Hearing these words, the Acharya rejoiced;
in His heart He knew: "My work is accomplished."

**84.** The Acharya says: "Lord, hear me:
all these people are devoted to Your feet.

**85.** You love Your devotees, You are an ocean of compassion:
giving them the treasure of prema, protect Your devotees."

**86.** Then all of them came to the Lord
and sat down around the Thakura.

**87.** The night was moonlit, the quarters shone;
looking at the Acharya, [the Lord] spoke again:

**88.** "Kamalaksha, You are My greatest devotee:
for Your sake I have come, for Your sake I have appeared.

**89.** Be happy singing My qualities and dancing;
may all people become devotees, seeing this."

**90.** Hearing these words, Srivasa Pandita,
with a serene heart, speaks before the Thakura:

**91.** "I wish to ask something — hear me;
I am afraid to speak, yet my heart is restless.

**92.** I have one doubt, and that is what I ask:
is this Advaita Acharya really Your devotee?"

**93.** Hearing this, Gaura Bhagavan, His face angry,
His eyes red with anger, began to rebuke him:

**94.** "Uddhava and Akrura are two dear ones of Mine —
and you think the Acharya lower than they?

**95.** Is there in Bharata-varsha
a single devotee of Mine equal to the Acharya?

**96.** Since you speak so, you are an ignorant brahmana:
I have no other devotee equal to the Acharya.

**97.** He is the king of the Vaishnavas, I call Him My own soul;
the creator of the world has come to deliver [the people of] Kali.

**98.** The shastras describe Him as Maha-Vishnu;
know that this Advaita is the avatara in the form of a devotee.

**99.** Therefore I tell you firmly:
always glorify the Acharya and be devoted to Him"[^27-8].

**100.** Hearing this, the brahmana was frightened at heart;
the brahmana fell silent, unable to utter a word.

**101.** Then Gaurahari says again:
"You will no longer speculate about the Self.

**102.** If I see or hear you speculating about the Self,
I shall no longer give you prema.

**103.** For one who disregards knowledge and karma, love for Krishna comes;
knowing this, do not take refuge in knowledge and karma."

**104.** Hearing this, Srivasa Pandita says:
"Give me this boon: let my heart forget it."

**105.** Murari said: "I do not know the doctrine of the Self."
The Lord says: "You know it — from Kamalaksha![^27-9]

**106.** With a pure heart be firmly devoted to Krishnachandra:
before the rasa of bhakti, liberation is a maidservant."

**107.** Hearing these words, all rejoiced
and in their hearts accepted the order: "We shall carry it out."

**108.** Intoxicated with the honey of the lotus feet of Hari-Hara,
they dance in joy like gods[^27-10].

**109.** Such is the wondrous story of the pastimes in Nadia:
Lochana has told how Gora preached prema.

*Raga sindhura*

**110.** His eyes are red lotuses, the pupils bees
drowning in the nectar of compassion.
His face is the full moon: at its radiance the heart weeps;
how many beginnings of prema are in it!

*Refrain: "The town of Nadia rejoices: swaying under the weight of prema, the moon, Shachi's darling, dances. They cry 'Jaya, jaya!' and auspicious words; seeing Him, all are amazed — the King of dancers, the enchanter of Madana!"*

**111.** His body is full of thrill, with drops of sweat upon it;
the hairs on end — like a golden kadamba.
At the onset of prema His body is like the morning sun;
His conch-like throat holds back half-spoken words.

**112.** In the fragrance of His lotus feet, ringed by ten moons of nails,
above them shine golden anklets.
When He walks, shining, lightning flashes;
the assembly of the gods is astonished.

**113.** In the midst of the earth of seven continents Navadvipa is adorned,
and in it a new manifestation of prema;
there the new Gaurahari sings the kirtana of Hari's qualities —
earth and sky rejoice.

**114.** Like a lion cub, He roars deeply again and again;
"hum!" — the waves of the ocean of prema.
He cries "Hari, Hari!" — and the world fell into rapture,
and the wives of noble houses forgot the honour of both houses[^27-11].

**115.** By the radiance of His body the sun is like a lamp;
in it is the play of lila and attire.
His charming body surpasses millions of flower-bowed Kamadevas;
in it He reveals prema.

**116.** The form of His face surpasses hundreds of thousands of full moons,
and on it are lovely moon-marks of sandal.
When the corners of His eyes move, nectar streams forth:
even those deluded from birth obtain prema.

**117.** His gait is that of an intoxicated elephant, He is wholly in the grip of bhava;
now He laughs, now starts and looks around.
His appearance enchants women; seeing Him, the whole land lost its head,
seeing [Him], they feel the pain of love.

**118.** To what shall I compare Him — the essence of compassion embodied?
Such is my Gora-raya.
In prema the people of Nadia know neither night nor day;
joyfully sings Lochana Dasa.

[^27-1]: Vv. 36–46 (as in Murari, 2.5.20–28) set out the path of knowledge (jnana-yoga) as those seeking liberation understand it; the Lord expounds it in order then to place bhakti above everything (vv. 47–49) and to forbid speculation about the Self (vv. 101–106). In v. 42 the 1983 edition has "without bhakti" instead of "without liberation". According to the teaching of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (and of Bhaktisiddhanta), Krishna is reached by bhakti alone ("bhaktyaham ekaya grahyah", Bhagavata 11.14.21), and liberation is not a precondition but a by-product of devotion.
[^27-2]: "Knowledge" here is the understanding that Krishna is Bhagavan Himself; it leads to firm faith and to causeless, uninterrupted bhakti (cf. Bhagavata 1.2.7). Cf. Murari 2.5.29–30.
[^27-3]: Cf. the Brahma-samhita (5.29, 5.56) and en/24, note 11. On Lakshmi's longing for the rasa of Vraja see Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 9. Vv. 56–58 — cf. Murari 2.5.30–33.
[^27-4]: Cf. Murari 2.6.1–6. The hater of devotees is perhaps the brahmana Gopala Chapala, struck with leprosy for offending Srivasa (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 17).
[^27-5]: Cf. Murari 2.6.7–9. "To go to the forest" hints at the coming sannyasa.
[^27-6]: According to Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition, the Lord descended in answer to the loud calls (hunkaras) and worship of Advaita, who offered tulasi leaves and Ganga water to the Shalagrama (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 3). Cf. Murari 2.6.10–13.
[^27-7]: The "children" are Advaita's sons (Achyutananda and others) or His disciples. Cf. Murari 2.6.14–15.
[^27-8]: Cf. Murari 2.6.16–22. So too in Gaudiya Vaishnava theology: Advaita is Maha-Vishnu, who has taken the form of a devotee (bhakta-avatara); Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 6. Kamalaksha is Advaita's worldly name.
[^27-9]: That is, "you heard it from Advaita". According to the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya 19), Advaita for a time deliberately preached the doctrine of the Self (the Yoga-vasishtha) so as to provoke the Lord's punishment. Cf. Murari 2.6.23–26.
[^27-10]: "Hari-Hara" — so also in Murari (2.6.27); probably Hari is Gaura, and Hara is Advaita, revered as one with Sadashiva.
[^27-11]: That is, they cast aside the honour of both their fathers' and their husbands' houses — an image from the songs of the gopis; cf. Lochana Dasa's "nagari-bhava" (en/09, note 9).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 5 (portion 28)

*The section "Meeting Nityananda": the name of Srivasa; Murari's Raghuvira-ashtaka and the name "Ramadasa"; service to Srivasa is worship of the Lord; the search for Nityananda and the meeting in the house of Nandana Acharya; the glory of Nityananda; Shachi accepts Nityananda as her son; in Srivasa's house the Lord reveals to Nityananda His six-armed, four-armed and two-armed forms*

## Meeting Nityananda

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One day Sriman Mahaprabhu asked Srivasa Pandita the meaning of his name and Himself revealed its sense: "Srivasa is the abode (avasa) of devotion (shri)." Then, when at the Lord's command Murari recited the Raghuvira-ashtaka, Sriman Mahaprabhu wrote the name "Ramadasa" on his forehead and showed him the form of Rama whom he worshipped. Having instructed Sri Rama Pandita to serve his brother Srivasa, a supreme devotee (parama-bhagavata), Sriman Mahaprabhu sent the devotees to look for Sriman Nityananda Prabhu. [Learning] that Sriman Nityananda Prabhu was staying in the house of Nandana Acharya, Mahaprabhu came there with His associates and met Him; before everyone He glorified the greatness of Nityananda Prabhu and [explained] the way to prema-bhakti for Krishna. When Sriman Nityananda Prabhu was taking His meal in the house of Srivasa Pandita, Sriman Mahaprabhu revealed to Him His six-armed, four-armed and two-armed forms[^28-1].

*Raga — any (yatha-raga); disha: "Ah, my life — Gorachand!"*

**1.** Then the Lord was sitting at home on a divine seat,
and around Him were His devotees.

**2.** Seeing Srivasa, the Lord said:
"Do you know how your name is formed?

**3.** You are the very abode (avasa) of Shri — of devotion;
that is why I say: your name is Srivasa."[^28-2]

**4.** Then, seeing Gopinatha, the Lord said:
"You are My devotee — go about with Me."[^28-3]

**5.** Seeing Murari, the Lord says again:
"Recite your verses — I shall listen to you."

**6.** Hearing these words, the skilful Murari
recites his own poetry, and the Thakura listens.

*So in the Sri Chaitanya-charita composed by Sri Murari Gupta, second prakrama, seventh sarga:*

**7.** "The rays of the gems of His gleaming crown light up the quarters; He wears earrings like the rising Brihaspati and Kavi; His face is like a spotless moon. Rama, the teacher of the three worlds, I worship ever.

**8.** His eyes are like lotuses awakened by the rays of the rising sun; His lips are like ripe bimba fruit, His nose is lovely; His charming smile outshines the moonbeams. Rama, the teacher of the three worlds, I worship ever."[^28-4]

**9.** Having thus heard the verses of the Raghuvira-ashtaka,
[the Lord] Himself placed His foot on Murari's head.

**10.** He wrote the name "Ramadasa" on his forehead:
"By My grace you have become Ramadasa.

**11.** Without Raghunatha you do not live even a moment;
know for certain: I am your Raghunatha."[^28-5]

**12.** Saying this, He showed him the form of Rama —
with Janaki, with all His associates and attendants.

**13.** Falling at His feet, Murari offers praise:
"Glory, glory to Raghuvira, the son of Shachi!"

**14.** Again and again he rises and falls, rolling on the ground,
offering many kinds of praise and humble entreaties.

**15.** The Lord mercifully said to Murari:
"Know nothing but devotion to Me.

**16.** Though your chosen Deity is I, Raghunatha,
still, taste the rasa of the Lord of Radha.

**17.** Go and sing of Radha and Krishna, following the dharma of sankirtana —
thus you will render devotion to Me; listen attentively."[^28-6]

**18.** Saying this, He Himself recited a verse:
"Hear one verse of Mine, brahmana Srinivasa."

*So [in the Srimad-Bhagavata (11.14.20)]:*

**19.** "Neither yoga nor Sankhya nor dharma, O Uddhava, neither Vedic study nor austerity nor renunciation win Me over as does mighty devotion to Me."

**20.** Having recited it, He said: "Hear, all My own:
act in this way.

**21.** Following the words of Srinivasa Pandita,
render devotion to Me — you will find great happiness.

**22.** Sri Rama Pandita, hear My words:
service to your elder brother is worship of Me.

**23.** Knowing this, serve Srivasa:
through this you will attain the radiance of My feet."[^28-7]

**24.** So spoke the Lord, affectionate to His devotees;
His eyes, reddened with compassion, brimmed with tears.

**25.** Then the skilful Srinivasa Pandita
offered milk, and the Thakura partakes of it.

**26.** Scents, sandal, garlands, fragrant betel,
incense and lamps he offered before Him.

**27.** The Lord joyfully accepted [the offerings]
and gave the remnants to all His devotees.[^28-8]

**28.** Thus in joyous play the whole night passed;
at dawn the Lord rose and went home.

**29.** All performed their bathing and worship of the Deity in their own homes
and came again to see His lotus feet.

**30.** Laughing, the Lord said: "Hear a wonderful thing:
Shripada Nityananda Avadhuta has come!

**31.** Who knows how to tell the truth of His glory?
By great merit and fortune I shall see His feet today.

**32.** Ho, Rama, Narayana, Murari, Mukunda,
quickly find out where Nityananda is."[^28-9]

**33.** When Mahaprabhu gave this order,
they set off at once and searched south of the village.

**34.** They searched but found no trace of Him
and came back again to His lotus feet.

**35.** With joined palms they say before the Thakura:
"Lord, we searched but found no trace of Him."

**36.** Again the Lord says: "Hear, all of you:
now go, each of you, to your own dwelling."[^28-10]

**37.** At the Lord's command all set off at once
and one by one went to their own homes.

**38.** In the evening, having performed the evening prayers, they gathered together
and all came together to the Lord.

**39.** Going along the road, the Lord calls: "Murari!" —
"You did not see the Avadhuta," He says, and laughs gently.

**40.** "The great soul is staying in the house of Nandana Acharya;
I too shall go there," He said firmly.

**41.** Hearing these words, all rejoiced
and went with the Thakura, crying: "Jaya, jaya!"

**42.** Going along the road, again and again He cries: "Hari, Hari!";
His cheeks thrill, His voice falters.

**43.** Tears stream from His eyes in many streams;
from prema the golden Youth cannot walk.

**44.** Now with a lion's prowess He goes four steps —
like a maddened elephant that does not look back;

**45.** now, like the deep rumble of a new raincloud,
again and again He roars "hum!", intoxicated with bliss.

**46.** So in joy and delight He goes on,
and He saw the Avadhuta Nityananda-raya.

**47.** His body is golden with a reddish glow, supremely beautiful;
an enchanting body in glittering ornaments.

**48.** Yellow cloth shines on His hips,
on His head a loosely tied turban of champaka hue.

**49.** As He walks, the bells on His feet are heard jingling,
setting the hearts of doe-eyed women aquiver.

**50.** When He laughs, it is as if lightning were falling from the sky:
women give up their modesty to it then and there.

**51.** His roar is heard, deeper than thunder —
a lion's roar that tames the maddened elephant of Kali.

**52.** His gait is beautiful, like a maddened elephant's;
on His serene face are ceaseless streams of prema.

**53.** His body is overcome with thrills, brimming with prema;
in the bhavas of trembling, perspiration and others He is attached to rasa.

**54.** In His hand is a golden staff that subdues the pride of Kali;
His charming palms are like red lotuses.

**55.** Armlets, bracelets, necklace, upper-arm bands, a belt of bells;
by His cheeks earrings shine like the sun.[^28-11]

**56.** Falling again and again, He rises crying: "Steady!"
and asks everyone: "Where is Kanai the cowherd?"

**57.** In an unearthly childlike mood He now weeps, now laughs;
now He cries: "Give Me honey!" — now praises Revati.[^28-12]

**58.** Now, with feet together, He goes by leaps;
He says one thing and does another — there is no understanding it.

**59.** By the fragrance of His body all the young women
then gave up the pride of noble wives.[^28-13]

**60.** The Lord, falling to the ground, bowed down to Him
and offered auspicious praise in sweet words.[^28-14]

**61.** Nityananda-raya fell at the Lord's feet:
each seeks to clasp the other's feet.

**62.** Both embrace, weeping and weeping,
and, gazing into each other's faces, laugh: "Where have You been?"

**63.** "I have wandered through the whole earth,
but nowhere did I find any trace of You.

**64.** I heard that in the land of Gauda, in the town of Navadvipa,
the son of Nanda is hiding.

**65.** To catch the thief I have come here —
I have caught the thief! Where will You flee now?"[^28-15]

**66.** Saying this, Nityananda laughs, weeps and dances,
and Gauranga dances in joy beside Nityananda.

**67.** "I have found Nityananda, the tamer of Kali's pride —
now the fallen, the lame, the dull and the blind are as good as delivered by Me!

**68.** By the might of Nityananda the three worlds are purified,
yet the godless, the wicked and the foolish do not know it.

**69.** But they too will all fall later into Nityananda's net" —
so spoke the Lord Gorachand.

**69+1.** Prostrating Himself on the ground, the Lord bows down [to Him]
and speaks humble words in auspicious syllables[^28-16].

**70.** Joyfully He sings the sankirtana of Hari's qualities:
He Himself dances, and with Him dances Nityananda.

**71.** Ending the dance, the two sat down,
and everyone gazed at Them in joy.

**72.** Then [the Lord] Himself took the dust of Nityananda's lotus feet
and placed it on the devotees' heads.

**73.** Receiving the dust of Nityananda's feet, the devotees —
their hearts overflowing with prema, tears streaming from their eyes.

**74.** So they stayed there a while in joy,
and the Lord, Shachi's son, went home.

**75.** Going along the road, He tells of Nityananda's glory:
"In all the three worlds there is none to compare with Him.

**76.** Hear, hear, all of you, My words:
this prema-bhakti of His for Krishna is not ordinary.

**77.** First there is knowledge, then devotion arises,
and then comes detachment from all enjoyments.

**78.** So step by step it grows day by day;
attachment to Krishna grows, and one becomes mature."[^28-17]

**79.** On another day Mahaprabhu
invited Nityananda, the best of sannyasis, to His own house.

**80.** After the meal He anointed His body with sandal
and, according to the rules of worship, offered Him a divine garland.[^28-18]

**80+1.** Taking His kaupina, the Lord tore it into pieces
and tied [them] on the heads of the devotees[^28-19].

**80+2.** He gave the devotees the water of His feet to drink;
seeing the Avadhuta, everyone's joy increased.

**80+3.** He dances and sings, He roars and thunders,
and sees the endless worlds filled with prema.

**80+4.** Nityananda Mahaprabhu, in supreme delight,
looking at Gaurachandra's face, laughs aloud.

**80+5.** Under the stamping of His feet the earth cannot stay still:
everyone took it for an earthquake.

**80+6.** Gaurachandra Prabhu, the Master of all, dances;
moment by moment the abundant waves of prema swell.

**80+7.** Seeing this, Shachi-devi rejoiced at heart:
looking at Nityananda, she recognized Vishvarupa [in Him].

**80+8.** Together with her daughter-in-law she holds a great festivity in the house:
auspicious cries of "ulu-ulu" and "Jaya!"

**80+9.** Seeing Vishvarupa in Nityananda today,
the goddess gazes [at Him] fixedly, her heart rejoicing.

**80+10.** Gaurachandra says: "Listen, dear one:
this is Vishvarupa — that very son, your own [son].

**80+11.** Under the name of Nityananda He has come to Navadvipa;
keep Him by you, [like] your dear Vishvambhara."

**80+12.** As soon as He said this, the goddess, plunged in an ocean of bliss,
wants to take Nityananda on her lap.

**80+13.** "Come, my son Vishvarupa!" — and she kisses His face.
"For joy I know not what is happening to my heart!"

**80+14.** Gaurachandra says: "Mother, do not be agitated;
keep this secret — listen to My word."

**80+15.** Nityananda Mahaprabhu at the feet of Ai (mother Shachi)
prostrates Himself with care.

**80+16.** With both hands He takes the dust of her feet
and, rejoicing, dances to Ai's delight.

**80+17.** After a while all together grew calm;
Nityananda Mahaprabhu [was] full of great merriment.

**81.** Seeing Nityananda, Shachi's eyes were soothed;
beside herself with love, she gazes at His face.

**82.** The Lord says: "Know Him as your own son
and care for Him more than for Me."

**83.** As on a son, Shachi gazes at Nityananda's face.
"You have become my son," says Shachi-devi.

**84.** "Be kind yourself to my Vishvambhara:
from today you two are my sons."

**85.** As she speaks, tears stream from Shachi's eyes,
and Shachi takes Nityananda in her arms as a son.

**86.** Nityananda, seeing her as His mother, prostrated Himself
at Shachi's feet and speaks in sweet words:

**87.** "All that you have said, mother, is true:
I am your son — know it for certain.

**88.** Do not hold your son's offences against Him, mother:
know it in every way — I am truly your son."

**89.** Receiving Nityananda's love as a son, queen Shachi —
tears flow from her eyes, her voice falters.

**90.** Thus all overflow with the rasa of affection;
seeing her two sons, Shachi was comforted at heart.[^28-20]

**91.** On another day Srivasa Pandita offered a meal,
and the Avadhuta took the meal in his house.

**92.** He found great satisfaction with the Pandita
and, having taken the meal, stayed there that day.

**93.** At that very moment Mahaprabhu Gaura Bhagavan
came with a serene face to Srivasa's house.

**94.** Entering the shrine room, He sits on a divine seat
and said: "Behold Me now with Your eyes."

**95.** Hearing these words, Nityananda, the best of sannyasis,
lovingly gazes at the form of Vishvambhara.

**96.** But He did not grasp any special meaning in this —
what purpose the Lord had hinted at.

**97.** Then Mahaprabhu Vishvambhara again,
looking at His own, said to them in reply:

**98.** "All of you, go out of this room" —
and they went out of the room, obeying the command.

**99.** What He says of Himself in the remaining words —
the inner meaning spoken in private — who will know?

**100.** He said: "Behold Me now Yourself:
for My sake You have toiled so much."

**101.** First the Lord showed His six-armed form,
then the four-armed form, and then the two-armed.

**102.** Seeing such a form, the greatest of wonders,
Nityananda Avadhuta remembered the past.[^28-21]

**103.** He saw: my Lord revealed Himself
and in one body showed three avataras.

**104.** He saw the divine forms of Rama, Krishna and Gauranga,
and afterwards saw the youthful pair — Radha and Kanu.[^28-22]

**105.** In delight Prabhu dances, His bliss boundless;
He knows no directions — an ocean of prema.

**106.** Hear, all of you, this wondrous story:
this servant Lochana tells the song of Gora's qualities.

*Raga — any (yatha-raga); refrain: "Hari, Rama, Narayana — golden Gora, Shachi's darling! At the festival of Nityananda's joy the enraptured devotees dance"*

**106+1.** A supremely wondrous story, unknown to people —
hear it, all you devotees, with one-pointed mind[^28-23].

**106+2.** Nityananda, so graceful in His play, sees the six-armed [form];
Nityananda's happiness grows — a heap of nectar.

**106+3.** In the two upper hands He sees bow and arrows;
in the two middle ones — the flute, [raised] at the breast to the lips.

**106+4.** In the two lower hands shine the waterpot and the staff.
Seeing this, [Nityananda] fiercely slaps His arms[^28-24].

**106+5.** The captivating sweetness of Rama, Krishna and Gauranga;
the crown of youths — a body full of rasa.

**106+6.** Seeing this, Nityananda Prabhu, His heart glad,
entering the mood of the lila, overflowed with the rasa of Gaura.

**106+7.** Then again and again He gives a deep roar;
in the build of His body He is just like the intoxicated Baladeva.

**106+8.** At the sight of that form Kamadeva swoons:
what on earth could be compared to it?

**106+9.** The pair of feet outshines red lotuses:
the bee-like devotees crave them in great excitement.

**106+10.** On them golden anklets shine captivatingly;
on the toes ten moons are resplendent.

**106+11.** Thighs like inverted plantain trunks, beautiful hips;
a blue dhoti neatly tied — waves of merriment.

**106+12.** Three folds [on the belly], a lovely deep navel:
seeing this, the hearts of the discerning nagaris lose their composure[^28-25].

**106+13.** On the broad, high chest — strings of pearls;
at the sight of the necklace of "elephant pearls" Kama swoons.

**106+14.** The neck a conch, the cheeks golden mirrors;
seeing this, wives of good families abandon shame and restraint.

**106+15.** In the ears — beautiful earrings like discs of the sun:
seeing them, the lotuses in the water open.

**106+16.** On His head a turban is loosely tied;
heavy with honey, His gaze — red lotuses — sways[^28-26].

**106+17.** "Shridama, Sudama, Dama, Vasudama, brothers!
Varuni, varuni!" — He calls, greatly intoxicated.

**106+18.** On the forehead a lovely tilaka of sandal paste;
the pair of brows has surpassed Kama's bow.

**106+19.** Millions of moons are offered up for that moon-like face;
streams of prema from the eyes — a shower of nectar.

**106+20.** In His holy hand an iron staff to crush the godless,
like the holy plough and club to destroy enemies.

**106+21.** Now He calls: "Dhavali! Shyamali!" —
"Brother Kanai, bring Me honey!"

**106+22.** Now with a voice of thunder He says "Hari, Hari!" —
now in supreme madness [of prema] He calls, "Brother, brother!"

**106+23.** Now, in the happiness of the rasa of bhakti, in keeping with the lila,
the two of Them bow to each other.

**106+24.** Nityananda Raya fell at the Lord's feet:
"Gaurachandra, grant me the bliss of prema!"

**106+25.** At Nityananda's feet falls Sri Gauranga Raya;
each of the Two seeks to clasp the other's feet.

**106+26.** In a choked voice Gora says: "Elder brother Balai!
Leaving Me, where did You go, brother?

**106+27.** In this guise, through what lands have You wandered so long?
Where have You left the herdsman's stick and the gunja garland?

**106+28.** What We were, what We have become — what has the Creator done!
Where is father Nanda, where is mother Yashomati?

**106+29.** Along the banks of the Kalindi We grazed the cows —
do You remember anything of it, elder brother Balai?"

**106+30.** Thus the two Lords met;
in bliss this servant Lochana sings of [Their] glories.

[^28-1]: The summary covers the whole section (vv. 1–106). Some places in the OCR of the edition are illegible and have been restored by sense (in square brackets). The section as a whole follows Murari Gupta (2.7–2.8).
[^28-2]: So also in Murari (2.7.7): "Shri is devotion, and you are its abode."
[^28-3]: Gopinatha is a brahmana of Navadvipa, an associate of the Lord; in Murari (2.7.8): "You are known as My servant."
[^28-4]: Vv. 7–8 are the first two stanzas of Murari Gupta's Ramashtaka (eight verses on Rama) (Murari 2.7.10–11); Brihaspati and Kavi (Shukra) are the planets Jupiter and Venus. Murari is a devotee of Rama, in Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition Hanuman (en/24, note 12). Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya and translation of the verses are not translated here or below.
[^28-5]: Cf. Murari 2.7.18. Ramadasa means "servant of Rama".
[^28-6]: This instruction is not in Murari. In the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 15.137–157) the Lord, testing Murari, advises him to worship Krishna, and then glorifies his unshakeable fidelity to Rama. Cf. also en/24, where the Lord instructs Murari to worship Radha and Krishna.
[^28-7]: The verse is Bhagavata 11.14.20; vv. 18–23 — cf. Murari 2.7.19–23. Sri Rama Pandita is the younger brother of Srivasa (Srinivasa).
[^28-8]: Cf. Murari 2.7.25–27; in Murari the remnants of the meal are received also by Narayani, Srivasa's niece, the future mother of Vrindavana Dasa.
[^28-9]: Cf. Murari 2.8.1–9. Shripada is a respectful address to a sannyasi; an Avadhuta is a wandering renunciant who stands above external rules. Rama is Sri Rama Pandita; Mukunda is Mukunda Datta. According to the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya-khanda), Nityananda came to Navadvipa after many years of pilgrimage and stayed in the house of Nandana Acharya.
[^28-10]: Literally "consider, all, your own dwelling"; so in A and in the 1983 edition. We understand it from v. 37 and from Murari (2.8.6–7), where the Lord says the Avadhuta can be found in the evening and sends everyone home.
[^28-11]: Vv. 47–55 describe Nityananda. According to Gaudiya Vaishnava theology Nityananda is Balarama Himself, Krishna's elder brother (cf. Murari 2.9.12); His staff, roar and gait "subdue Kali" — that is, His mercy breaks the power of the age of Kali. The yellow cloth is Lochana Dasa's; in Murari (2.9.12) Balarama wears blue.
[^28-12]: Nityananda is in the mood of Balarama: Kanai is Krishna, His younger brother; Revati is Balarama's consort; "honey" (madhu) is the intoxicating honey-wine (varuni) that Balarama drinks (cf. Murari 2.9.12, 2.14). This intoxication is the ecstasy of prema, not worldly drunkenness; the Avadhuta's strange behaviour (vv. 56–58) is likewise a manifestation of prema.
[^28-13]: An image from the songs of the gopis, as in Lochana Dasa's descriptions of Gaura (cf. en/09, note 9, on "nagari-bhava"): the beauty of the Lord and His associates draws souls away from worldly attachments; it is not worldly passion.
[^28-14]: Cf. Murari 2.8.10–13: the Lord bows to Nityananda with devotion. Thus the Lord honours Nityananda as His elder brother (Balarama) and shows how to honour a Vaishnava.
[^28-15]: Nityananda had wandered for many years through the holy places in search of Krishna, and found Him "hiding" in Navadvipa in the form of Gaura. The "thief" is Krishna, the Stealer of butter and of hearts.
[^28-16]: V. 69+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it); it nearly repeats v. 60.
[^28-17]: Vv. 75–78 — cf. Murari 2.8.15–16. "Knowledge" here is the knowledge of Krishna as Bhagavan Himself (cf. en/27, note 2); according to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, devotion itself brings both knowledge and detachment (Bhagavata 1.2.7; 11.2.42).
[^28-18]: Cf. Murari 2.8.18–19.
[^28-19]: Vv. 80+1 — 80+17 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition gives vv. 80+1 — 80+16 in a footnote as an additional reading. By distributing pieces of the Avadhuta's kaupina to the devotees and giving them the water of His feet to drink, the Lord teaches them to honour Nityananda as the supreme guru (cf. the Chaitanya-bhagavata, Madhya-khanda, where the Lord likewise has the devotees tie pieces of Nityananda's kaupina on their heads). Shachi recognizes in Nityananda her elder son Vishvarupa: according to Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding, Nityananda is Balarama Himself and Vishvarupa an expansion of Sankarshana, and tradition holds that Vishvarupa, on leaving this world, entered into Nityananda; cf. also the Chaitanya-bhagavata, where Shachi sees Nityananda and Nimai in a dream as Balarama and Krishna. Vv. 80+10 — 80+11 are not quite clear in either edition (lit. "listen, my father" — an endearment; "Vishvarupa — that son, your uterine…"; "keep my dear Vishvambhara near you"); they are translated by sense.
[^28-20]: Vv. 81–90 are not in Murari. Nityananda, according to Gaudiya Vaishnava theology, is Balarama, and Vishvarupa too is an expansion of Sankarshana; hence Shachi naturally loves Nityananda as her son.
[^28-21]: Vv. 91–102 — cf. Murari 2.8.20–29. "The past" is His former pastimes with Krishna as Balarama (in Murari, 2.8.29, the Lord says: "You are My brother who sported in Vrindavana!"). In Murari Nityananda tells no one of what He saw.
[^28-22]: The six-armed form unites Rama (bow and arrows), Krishna (flute) and Gaura the sannyasi (staff and waterpot); by tradition, the Lord also revealed the same form to Sarvabhauma (en/45, vv. 243–245 and note 22; according to the Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 6, Sarvabhauma sees a four-armed and then a two-armed form). The vision of Radha and Krishna in Gaura: according to Gaudiya Vaishnava theology, Gaura is Radha and Krishna in one body (cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 4; Madhya 8, Ramananda Raya's vision).
[^28-23]: The song of vv. 106+1 — 106+30 is found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition, where a new section begins at once); the 1903 edition gives it in a footnote as an additional reading. Nityananda sees the six-armed form (see note 22) and then abides in the bhava of Balarama: He calls the cowherd boys and cows of Vraja and asks Kanai (Krishna) for honey. This bhava is His own: according to Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding, Nityananda is Balarama Himself (the comparison "just like the intoxicated Baladeva" in v. 106+7 is poetic). In vv. 106+26 — 106+29 the Lord recalls with Him the pastimes in Vraja, as Krishna with His elder brother.
[^28-24]: Slapping one's arms (malasat) is the gesture of a wrestler challenging to a fight; this is Balarama's heroic mood.
[^28-25]: Vv. 106+12 — 106+14 are Lochana Dasa's nagari-bhava motif, here transferred to Nityananda (see en/09, note 9); the subject is the Lord's beauty that captivates hearts, not worldly passion.
[^28-26]: Honey (madhu) and varuni (106+17) are the intoxicating drink Balarama loves; Nityananda's "intoxication" is the ecstasy of prema, not worldly intoxication (cf. note 12).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 6 (portion 29)

*The section "Meeting with Advaita and Haridasa": the Lord weeps after seeing Krishna in a dream; Nityananda beholds the Lord's forms; Nityananda at Advaita's; Advaita worships the Lord in Srivasa's house; the arrival of Haridasa; Nityananda's kaupina; the Lord vanishes with Srivasa and returns; the greatness of prema*

## Meeting with Advaita and Haridasa

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One night, in the third watch, Sriman Mahaprabhu suddenly began to weep; Shachi-devi in alarm asked the reason for His weeping, and Mahaprabhu told His mother how He had seen Krishna in a dream. Sriman Nityananda Prabhu [beheld] the six-armed and other forms of Mahaprabhu; then at Mahaprabhu's command the devotees took Him to Advaita's [house], and there They spent two days in great bliss. [When] Murari [told the Lord] of Nityananda Prabhu's manifestations of prema, [the Lord laughed]. When Advaita [Prabhu], seeing Sriman Mahaprabhu in the house of Srivasa Pandita, worshipped Him, the Vaishnavas [danced in bliss]. [Thakura] Haridasa came [and met the Lord]. When Nityananda Prabhu [was taking leave] of Sriman Mahaprabhu, Mahaprabhu took Nityananda's kaupina [and distributed it] to the devotees, [and they tied it] on their heads. [When the Lord disappeared,] the devotees lamented, and when He returned, they were seized with boundless bliss.[^29-1]

*Raga turi; refrain: "Gora has remembered the past — that is why Gora weeps!"*[^29-2]

**1.** Now I shall tell another wondrous story —
of such deeds of Gora as none has seen or heard.

**2.** The Lord of all worlds has descended on earth —
why do you not count this your good fortune?

**3.** Cunning does not leave the vile hearts of the godless:
their hearts are entangled in this maya of Vishnu.[^29-3]

**4.** They will become pure when they hear of Gora's qualities:
this is the means that cures the disease of worldly existence.

**5.** One day, as the third watch of the night was passing,
Vishvambhara suddenly began to weep.

**6.** Astonished, Shachi asks her son:
"Why are you weeping, child? Tell me.

**7.** Hearing your weeping, I burn all over;
I cannot hold my heart — an arrow pierces my breast."

**8.** Hearing His mother's words, He is silent,
and then, sitting up on the bed, tells the dream He saw:

**9.** "I saw a Person shining like a fresh raincloud,
with a crest of peacock feathers, wondrously radiant;

**10.** bracelets, armlets, a necklace, bells on His feet,
on His forehead a moon of sandal, richly shining;

**11.** clad in yellow, with a flute in His left hand —
such a Beautiful One I saw, and My heart rejoiced."

**12.** He weeps, streams of tears flow from His eyes:
"Do not tell anyone, so that no one else may hear."

**13.** Hearing such words, the nectarean speech
risen from Vishvambhara's lips, Shachi rejoiced.

**14.** Vishvambhara's whole body is filled with thrills,
and the whole house glitters with the radiance of His body.[^29-4]

**15.** At that time Nityananda the Avadhuta-raya
came there from Srinivasa's house.

**16.** Coming, He saw the Lord's beautiful body:
radiant, mighty-armed, with a deep navel;

**17.** in His right hand a club, in His left hand a flute,
in His palm a lotus, in His left palm a bow;

**18.** His lustre like molten gold, the Kaustubha on His breast,
two makara-shaped earrings adorning His cheeks;

**19.** a necklace with the lustre of emerald adorning His neck —
such wondrous attire the Avadhuta-raya beholds.

**20.** Then He sees Him four-armed — without the bow and flute:
the form all the same, with four excellent arms.

**21.** A moment later He sees Him two-armed —
in a form and deeds gracious to the world.[^29-5]

**22.** Seeing this form, the Avadhuta-raya
embraces His own, dances and sings.

**23.** In rapture He dances, beside Himself,
plunged into the great ocean of prema.

**24.** [The Lord said:] "Srinivasa, Narayana, Rama, Murari,
go, the four of you, with Him.

**25.** The Avadhuta will go to the house of Advaita Acharya —
let Him know of it: He is a great wonder."

**26.** When Mahaprabhu gave this command,
the hearts of all who heard it rejoiced.

**27.** All set off quickly with Nityananda
and with joyful hearts came to the Acharya's house.

**28.** Bowing down, they told everything;
hearing it, the Acharya dances in bliss, beside Himself.

**29.** Both joyfully embrace each other:
the Acharya dances happily, Nityananda dances.

**30.** Happily They sank wholly into the ocean of bliss:
again and again roars of "hum!" rise up in waves.

**31.** Both speak confidentially of Gauranga's deeds;
telling and hearing, both are enraptured at heart.

**32.** So they spent two days in joy;
all the Vaishnavas joyfully sang of Gora's qualities.

**33.** Again respectfully asking leave at Advaita's feet,
they hurried to see Gaurahari.

**34.** Coming before the Lord and bowing down,
with joined palms Murari tells everything:

**35.** all the confidential play that took place in the Acharya's house;
hearing it, the Lord was delighted and laughed.[^29-6]

**36.** The next day the Acharya Himself, the best of brahmanas,
came to see His lotus feet.

**37.** In the house of Srinivasa Pandita Mahaprabhu
sits in the shrine room, smiling gently.

**38.** The Lord sits in great joy on a divine seat;
the room glitters with the radiance of His body.

**39.** The beauty of His holy body is like molten gold,
ruddy with prema, like the morning sun.

**40.** Divine ornaments, garlands, fragrant sandal;
His beautiful face surpasses the full moon.

**41.** Gadadhara and Narahari stand on both sides,
and Sri Raghunandana gazes at the moon of His face.[^29-7]

**42.** All around Him are the devotees,
like the shining moon in a ring of stars.

**43.** Nityananda, sitting before Him in the bliss of prema,
gazes at His face and again and again laughs and weeps.

**44.** At that very time, seeing [Him], the Acharya, the moon among brahmanas,
again and again roars "hum!" and lets out a lion's roar.

**45.** His body from feet to head filled with thrills;
the universe cannot contain the joy of His heart.

**46.** The brahmana offered various gifts
and laid new divine garments at His lotus feet.

**47.** With tulasi blossoms He worshipped His feet,
with a fragrant garland of malati and with fragrant sandal.

**48.** He prostrates, falling on the ground like a rod —
and Mahaprabhu Himself lifted Him up.

**49.** Accepting the worship, Gaura Bhagavan
gave the remnants as a gift to His devotees.

**50.** Those garments and ornaments adorn His holy body;
crying "Hari, Hari!", He dances with them all.

**51.** Advaita Acharya and Nityananda-raya,
Srinivasa, Murari, Mukunda sing His qualities.

**52.** All the Vaishnavas together in joyful exultation
forget themselves, absorbed in rasa.

**53.** All praise one another: "Blessed, blessed!" —
and deem the happiness of kaivalya and nirvana insignificant.

**54.** In the happiness of prema's bliss they know neither day nor night,
ever overwhelmed with inner joy.

**55.** The dance begins at sunrise and lasts into the night;
from evening they dance until the next dawn.[^29-8]

**56.** So night and day they are immersed in the bliss of prema.
When the dance ended, Gora commanded all:

**57.** "Bathe and worship the Deity, all of you, in your own homes,
and after your meal come again."

**58.** So all, having completed their rites,
came and assembled at His lotus feet.

**59.** At that very time the great-souled Haridasa,
whose heart ceaselessly exults in the name of Krishna —

**60.** a bee intoxicated with the honey of Krishna's lotus feet,
who in absorption in rasa becomes a young lion —

**61.** suddenly arrived in Navadvipa.
"Come, come!" the Lord greets him, laughing.

**62.** With full love He embraced him tightly,
and Mahaprabhu bade him sit down.

**63.** The wise Haridasa bows down,
and the Thakura Himself, taking his hand, raises him.

**64.** He anointed his body with fragrant sandal
and gave him His own prasada garland from His body.

**65.** The Thakura bade him eat,
and he partook of abundant mahaprasada.[^29-9]

**66.** So Mahaprabhu with a joyful heart
delights in the sankirtana of Hari's name and qualities,

**67.** [with Him] Haridasa, Advaita Acharya, Nityananda,
Srinivasa and all the rest of His own.

**68.** Days and nights they passed in joyful play of prema's bliss.
[At last the Lord] dismissed the Acharya: "Go home today."

**69.** Receiving the command, Advaita Acharya went home,
lost in happiness at all He had seen and heard.

**70.** Then Nityananda the Avadhuta-raya
took leave of the Lord.

**71.** The Thakura went along to see Him off;
from love He cannot turn back and went far.

**72.** The Avadhuta-raya cannot bring Himself to leave [Him] and go;
with great effort He took His leave.

**73.** At the parting the Lord says:
"Give them all one kaupina."

**74.** At the Lord's word the Thakura Avadhuta
gave them all a wondrous kaupina.

**75.** The Lord Himself, laughing, took the kaupina,
divided it and gave it to all His devotees.

**76.** Receiving this kaupina joyfully as grace,
all, rejoicing, tied it on their heads.[^29-10]

**77.** Taking leave at Nityananda's lotus feet,
they go home together with the Lord.

**78.** All came home with sorrowful hearts
and sit in their houses, their eyes brimming with tears.

**79.** After a while, having bathed and worshipped the Deity,
in the evening all came to see Gaurahari.

**80.** And Nityananda went to Acharya Gosani
and joyfully speaks of Gora day and night.

**81.** Hear, all of you, the story of the next day —
then you will attain prema-bhakti at the feet of Sri Krishna.

**82.** It is a wondrous story, beyond the reach of the world and the Vedas;
this song of Gora's qualities is the essence of nectar.

**83.** Seeing His own, the Lord embraces them
and, hearing of His own qualities, moves about dancing.

**84.** All around, everyone happily dances and sings,
and in the middle, absorbed in bliss, Gora-raya dances.

**85.** Suddenly the Lord Vishvambhara, taking Srinivasa by the hand,
went off — no one knows where.

**86.** All around, everyone is dancing and singing,
but Mahaprabhu is not in the middle — He cannot be seen.

**87.** Fear arose in everyone's heart;
all the people weep, reckoning all lost.

**88.** Rolling on the ground they weep and find no calm;
all the people of Nadia weep, recalling His qualities.

**89.** Everyone runs about searching from house to house,
unable to open their eyes for the tears.

**90.** "We shall take poison and all die!
Why, where has our Lord Gora gone?"

**91.** So lament all His own.
Hearing it, Shachi ran [there], beside herself.

**92.** Without arranging her clothes, without tying her hair,
beating her breast, she runs, frantic with grief.

**93.** "Child, child!" she calls aloud to Vishvambhara. —
"Come home: it is already the second watch of the day!

**94.** Lamp of my family, moon of Nadia,
pupil of my eyes — who has blinded me?"

**95.** Seeing the extreme anguish of all,
the Lord, affectionate to His devotees, suddenly came.

**96.** As the sun rises in pitch darkness,
so the Lord illumined the hearts of the Vaishnavas.

**97.** Some, falling at His feet, weep with cries of anguish;
some, seeing His holy face, dance in rapture.

**98.** Some say: "Mahaprabhu, without Your feet
there is darkness in all ten directions — we see nothing with our eyes."

**99.** Shachi, beside herself with love, takes her son in her arms
and showers His lotus face with hundreds of thousands of kisses:

**100.** "You are my staff — the blind woman's — the pupil of both my eyes,
the soul of this body; apart from you I have no one.

**101.** My whole world had become empty —
with the rising of Gorachand the darkness vanished."[^29-11]

**102.** Murari, Mukunda Datta and Haridasa
humbly say: "Hear, Srinivasa:

**103.** the Lord has no servant dearer than you;
by your grace these feet have appeared [to us].

**104.** What can all of us say to you?
Considering [us] your own, be kind to us yourself."[^29-12]

**105.** Saying this, all together sing Hari's qualities,
and Gora-raya, intoxicated with love, dances.

**106.** Hear, all of you, this wondrous story:
in Navadvipa the jewel of love has been proclaimed.

**107.** The Lord's prema-bhakti is rare in the three worlds —
who has the power to attain it?

**108.** Lakshmi, Ananta, Shiva or Sanatana —
none of them knows the inner essence of this prema-bhakti.[^29-13]

**109.** Such prema-bhakti the Lord reveals —
so says with a joyful heart this Lochana Dasa.

[^29-1]: The summary covers the whole section (vv. 1–109). In the OCR of the edition it is almost illegible (lines of two columns are mixed); it has been restored from the surviving words and from the table of contents in Bhaktisiddhanta's preface (the fifth chapter of the Madhya-khanda); what is restored by sense is in square brackets. The section as a whole follows Murari Gupta (2.9–2.10).
[^29-2]: The raga is given only in the 1983 edition. The refrain is read after the 1903 edition (in the OCR of Bhaktisiddhanta's edition it is garbled); it refers to the Lord's weeping in vv. 5–12: Gora remembers His former pastimes — the pastimes of Krishna.
[^29-3]: Vishnu-maya here is the Lord's external energy, which by His will keeps the godless away from Him (cf. en/26, note 17); the Lord Himself is never subject to maya.
[^29-4]: Vv. 5–14 — cf. Murari 2.9.2–6, where the Lord sees the Boy Krishna in a dream. The request to tell no one: the Lord conceals His intimate bhava.
[^29-5]: Vv. 15–23 — cf. Murari 2.9.6–11: Nityananda sees the form of Hari with club, discus, conch, flute, bow and lotus, then the four-armed and two-armed forms. The translation of the second line of v. 20 is tentative.
[^29-6]: Vv. 24–35 — cf. Murari 2.9.13–16. Advaita lived in Shantipura, on the bank of the Ganga. Rama is Sri Rama Pandita; Narayana is an associate of the Lord in Navadvipa.
[^29-7]: Gadadhara is Radha (en/25, note 8). Narahari Sarakara is Lochana Dasa's guru, and Raghunandana his nephew, both of Shrikhanda; only Lochana Dasa mentions their presence in this scene. In Murari (2.9.17–18) Advaita sees the Lord on the seat of honour in Srivasa's shrine room.
[^29-8]: Vv. 44–55 — cf. Murari 2.9.17–22. Kaivalya and nirvana are liberation as merging into Brahman; for the devotees it is insignificant beside prema.
[^29-9]: Vv. 59–65 — cf. Murari 2.10.2–5. Haridasa Thakura is the great devotee of the holy name; the Lord Himself feeds him mahaprasada.
[^29-10]: Vv. 68–76 — cf. Murari 2.10.6–9. The kaupina is a renunciant's loincloth; the Lord gave the pieces of Nityananda's kaupina to the devotees as a holy relic.
[^29-11]: Vv. 83–101 — cf. Murari 2.10.10–13: the Lord carries Srivasa away from the circle of dancers and, seeing the devotees' alarm, appears again of Himself. Shachi's lament is only in Lochana Dasa.
[^29-12]: The devotees ascribe the Lord's return to the grace of Srivasa, whom He had taken with Him: thus the Lord's mercy comes through His devotee.
[^29-13]: Ananta (Shesha) is an expansion of the Lord; Sanatana is one of the four Kumaras (possibly also an epithet of Shiva, "the eternal Shiva"). Cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 8–9: even Lakshmi did not attain the rasa of the gopis; the prema that Gaura bestowed is the highest treasure, rare even for the greatest.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 7 (portion 30)

*The section "Pastimes with the Devotees and the Deliverance of Jagai and Madhai": the devotees place on their heads the water of Nityananda's feet; Haridasa in the form of Brahma; the arrival of Advaita; the Lord speaks of the reasons for His descent; He bids the devotees give the holy name from house to house; the sins of Jagai and Madhai; sankirtana through the town; the brothers wound Nityananda; the Lord summons Sudarshana, and Nityananda pleads for the offenders*

## Pastimes with the Devotees and the Deliverance of Jagai and Madhai

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One day, when Sriman Mahaprabhu was sporting in the bliss of prema with the devotees headed by Srivasa, Haridasa, Murari and Mukunda, Sriman Nityananda Prabhu came there. At Sriman Mahaprabhu's command the devotees placed on their heads the water of Sriman Nityananda Prabhu's feet and [began] to dance in the bliss of prema. When Haridasa and Sriman Advaita Prabhu came there, [Haridasa] offered Him praise; then Sriman Mahaprabhu rose together with the devotees and showed him due honour, fed him and told him of preaching prema without distinction between the worthy and the unworthy, and commanded His devotees to go from door to door and distribute the holy name and prema. But when the devotees refused, [fearing] two great sinful brahmanas named Jagai and Madhai, Sriman Mahaprabhu Himself glorified before the devotees the greatness of the holy name — for example, the deliverance of the great sinner Ajamila by namabhasa, [the name uttered while intending another,] — and, to deliver these two sons of brahmanas, expressed His wish to go round the town Himself with the devotees, performing kirtana with mridangas and karatalas. Delighted at Sriman Mahaprabhu's words, the devotees all gathered together and began kirtana; all around resounded with the holy name. Hearing the kirtana, Jagai and Madhai in great anger began to abuse Sriman Mahaprabhu in every way; [Sriman Mahaprabhu and] Nityananda by Their own will [paid no heed] and [looked on them with mercy]. At last they struck Sri Nityananda on the head with a shard of a pot. Sriman Mahaprabhu, unable to bear [the insult and pain] of His own [devotee], summoned Sudarshana to destroy those two. Sri Nityananda Prabhu forgave Jagai and Madhai all their offences and begged for their lives, [and Mahaprabhu] cast a merciful glance on them. [By the grace] of Sri Nityananda, Jagai and Madhai were delivered and became great devotees. Then the author glorified the compassion of Nityananda and Mahaprabhu.[^30-1]

*Raga dhanashi; refrain: "What a wonder in the midst of Nadia! Golden Gauranga dances — a great wonder!"*

**1.** Thus the Thakura sports in Navadvipa:
forgetting Himself, He reveals prema abundantly.

**2.** Independent as He is, He becomes subject to His devotees
and, as if most humble, begs everyone to take prema.

**2+1.** Suddenly one day, at a blessed and lovely hour,
He was playing with His own people in the evening[^30-2].

**2+2.** He snatched away everyone's clothes,
and all, laughing for joy, humbly beseech [Him].

**2+3.** For shame everyone's limbs went weak;
covering themselves with their hands, they coax [Him] again and again:

**2+4.** "Give the clothes, give the clothes, Lord of the three worlds!
It does not befit You, Lord, to act like this!"

**2+5.** Hearing these words, the Lord grew merrier still
and a moment later gave everyone back their clothes.

**2+6.** Thus plays the crown-jewel of connoisseurs of rasa,
the giver of rasa to all, who knows every rasa.

**2+7.** Giving back the clothes, He gladdened all His own people;
He Himself dances joyfully, and His servants dance.

**3.** The Lord moves in His lila unnoticed by people;
only His own understand His hints.

**4.** Srinivasa, Haridasa, Murari, Mukunda,
understanding His hint, sing, and the bliss of prema grows.

**5.** Overwhelmed with joy, His associates dance and sing;
at that time the Avadhuta-raya came again.

**6.** "The Avadhuta has come!" — "Jaya, jaya!" rings out,
and all the people joyfully sing auspicious songs.

**7.** His gait is slow, like a maddened elephant's;
hearing the cry "Hari, Hari!", He is not master of Himself.

**8.** Swaying, He walks, blocking the road;
after two steps He stops and looks all around.

**9.** His whole body thrills from feet to head:
each hair standing on end surpasses the kadamba's filaments.

**10.** With bent neck He looks to both sides with red eyes;
at times He runs in rapture, calling loudly.

**11.** So, with hundreds of people running behind Him,
absorbed in bliss He came to where Gora-raya was.

**12.** Seeing Nityananda, the Lord Gaurasundara [embraces Him]:
the two embrace, overflowing with prema.

**13.** Tears of prema's bliss flow from the eyes of both;
both are absorbed in bliss, Their bodies unsteady.

**14.** Both dance in joy, and with Them Their own,
like the cowherd boys with Krishna and Balarama.

**15.** When the dance ended, the Lord told everyone
to wash Nityananda's feet:

**16.** "Place on your heads the water of Nityananda's feet —
you will attain supreme prema, waves of bliss."

**17.** When Mahaprabhu gave this command,
the joy in the hearts of all who heard it grew.

**18.** They longed for one thing and received more — the Lord's command;
they placed on their heads the water that had washed [His] feet.

**19.** Then Avadhuta Prabhu, hearing the Lord's command —
tears brimmed in His reddened eyes.

**20.** Rising, He joyfully embraces everyone:
the ocean of prema surged in waves of bliss.

**21.** Overwhelmed by prema, all weep
and clasp the Avadhuta's feet to their hearts.

**22.** The great festival of prema grew boundlessly:
within — radiance, without — the transformations of ecstasy.

**23.** Seeing this, the Lord Gaura Bhagavan
looks on with a serene face, rejoicing at heart.[^30-3]

**24.** All, surrounding [Him] on every side, offer praise;
at that time Haridasa suddenly came:

**25.** on his neck a necklace of pure crystal,
golden bells tinkling on his red feet;

**26.** his whole body thrilled, his eyes full of tears,
his body swaying with prema, he roars "hum!" and calls aloud.

**27.** In the fullness of prema he dances before the Lord —
the universe cannot contain his happiness in prema's bliss.

**28.** [The Lord], by the custom of hospitality [offering] water for the feet, arghya and water for sipping,
Himself bade [him] take food.[^30-4]

**29.** At that time Advaita Acharya suddenly
came to the Lord.

**30.** The Thakura rose and honoured Him,
and all, rising, bowed to Him.

**31.** Dancing and dancing, [Haridasa], in whom Brahma manifested,
falls at the Lord's feet and prostrates;

**32.** with four mouths, reciting the Vedas, he offers praise.
"Be calm!" says the Lord, lifts him up and embraces him.

**33.** Becoming calm, Haridasa dances, weeps and laughs,
knowing no directions, floating in prema's bliss.[^30-5]

**34.** Then Acharya Gosani was filled with reverent awe
and, taking the command on his head, ate there.

**35.** So the Lord with all His own
sits secluded in the house, smiling gently.

**36.** Among His own the Lord speaks of Himself —
of the reasons for which He came to the earth:

**37.** "To taste My own bhava, to destroy irreligion,
to establish dharma and to reveal the kirtana of the name.

**38.** In every land, in every home, I will reveal
the bhava of Vraja — servitude, friendship, parental love and amorous love.

**39.** Above all I will give to taste the treasure of prema for Radha and Krishna:
I Myself will taste it and make the three worlds taste it.

**40.** To gods and asuras I will give this treasure of prema,
to chandalas, yavanas, the ignorant, women and children.

**41.** Bringing the happiness of Vrindavana to Nadia,
together with all of you I will give it to taste in every land."[^30-6]

**42.** A most wondrous story — the pastimes in Nadia;
all these stories I shall proclaim together.

**43.** Gadadhara and Narahari sit on both sides,
Sri Raghunandana delights near His feet;

**44.** Advaita Acharya and Nityananda-raya —
and the Thakura Himself sings the song of His own qualities;

**45.** Murari, Mukunda Datta and Srinivasa,
Haridasa and the rest — all abodes of prema;

**46.** Shuklambara, Vakreshvara, Shriman, Sanjaya,
Shridhara Pandita and the other great souls.[^30-7]

**47.** Who can tell the glory of even one of them?
Gauradeva Himself has descended to the earth!

**48.** The manifestation in Nadia has no comparison —
so says with a joyful heart this Lochana Dasa.

*Raga gurjari; disha: "Gorachand is my life!" (with vocables); refrain: "Hari, Rama, Narayana — golden Gora, Shachi's darling!"*

**49.** I shall tell an unprecedented story — hear, all:
whoever hears it is freed from all sins.

**50.** In Navadvipa, in His own home,
Gaurachandra is in joyful play with His disciples.

**51.** Gathering all His devotees together,
He was lost in prema's bliss in the sankirtana of His own qualities.

**52.** Laughing, the Lord said to all the devotees:
"Give this name of Hari of Mine in every home.

**53.** All who live in Navadvipa — children and old people,
chandalas and the wretched, the good and the wicked —

**54.** teach them all the name of Hari, tying it fast,
so that all people may easily cross the ocean of existence."[^30-8]

**55.** Hearing this, all the devotees said to the Lord:
"We cannot give the name of Hari in every home.

**56.** In this Navadvipa there is one calamity:
most wicked [men], whose sins have no end —

**57.** two brothers, greatly sinful brahmanas,
the masters of Navadvipa, Jagai and Madhai.[^30-9]

**58.** They spare neither brahmana women, nor Muslim women, nor the wives of gurus;
getting liquor, they abandon all duties.

**59.** They constantly do violence to gods, gurus and brahmanas;
when they go out, they do not return home without a killing.

**60.** Killings of brahmanas, cows, women — by hundreds;
I cannot write how many sins they have committed.

**61.** They live on the bank of the Ganga, yet do not bathe in the Ganga;
never in their lives have they worshipped the gods.

**62.** They constantly punish their own kin and friends;
towards the sankirtana of Krishna's qualities they are utter atheists.

**62+1.** Even if a thousand scribes were to write [them] down for a hundred births,
they would still not see the end of their sins"[^30-10].

**63.** One day, when the Lord was among His own,
in the course of conversation talk of them arose.

**64.** Everyone told [of them] before the Lord;
hearing it, the Lord grew angry, pondering within.

**65.** His face turned red, both eyes reddened:
"What you have said, My heart too bears witness to.

**66.** There was a sinful brahmana named Ajamila;
at the hour of death he uttered the name "Narayana".

**67.** Out of love for his son he uttered the name "Narayana" —
and the brahmana, receiving a divine body, attained Vaikuntha.[^30-11]

**68.** Jagai and Madhai are more sinful than he:
by what means will they be delivered?

**69.** For their sake My heart is pained;
all of you, hear what I say in answer.

**70.** The sankirtana of Hari's name is the dharma of the age of Kali:
by the sankirtana of the name and qualities I will accomplish the whole task.

**71.** Bring your friends, wherever they may be;
gathering all together, perform sankirtana.

**72.** Taking singers and players, mridangas and karatalas,
sing aloud the sweet kirtana of the name.

**73.** I will go round the town performing kirtana."
Hearing these words, all came:

**74.** Advaita Acharya and His people,
the Avadhuta Nityananda with a serene face,

**75.** Haridasa, Srinivasa with his four brothers,
Murari, Mukunda Datta, Pandita Gadai,

**76.** Sri Chandrashekhara Acharya and Shuklambara —
all together came to the Thakura's house.

**77.** All the devotees, wherever they were,
at the Lord's command gathered together.

**78.** When all had gathered and sankirtana began,
the Lord Vishvambhara Hari set forth.

**79.** In the town of Nadia waves of bliss arose;
the cry "Hari, Hari!" rises to the sky.

**80.** Meanwhile Jagai and Madhai lie in their house:
drunk on their liquor, both brothers sleep.

**81.** Along that very road the Lord goes performing kirtana,
and all the people of Nadia run to see.

**82.** In the roar of the kirtana — karatalas, mridangas and the rest —
on all sides one hears only "Hari, Hari!".

**83.** At the roar of the kirtana the two brothers awoke;
raising their heads they look and angrily shout: "Seize, seize!"

**84.** With reddened eyes they glare in anger:
"What noise is this we hear? As if a spear had struck our ears!

**85.** Every sound is like a spear in the heart.
If they value their lives, let them be silent!"

**86.** Their attendants say to them:
"Restrain yourselves, masters — with whom are you angry?

**87.** Give the order and we will go at once and forbid them;
who would then dare to come this way again?

**88.** It is Jagannatha's son, the brahmana Nimai Pandita,
performing kirtana surrounded by brahmanas."

**89.** "Forbid them: let them go another way!
Let them be silent if they value their lives.

**90.** They go about making a pointless din, not knowing the root of things —
at my hand they will lose caste, life and family!"

**91.** Saying this, they sent their messenger,
and he gave [their message] to the Thakura; Shachi's son listens —

**92.** and sings the sankirtana of Hari's name all the louder:
raising His arms, again and again He cries "Hari, Hari!".

**93.** Doubling the prema, He increases the exultation:
the cry "Hari, Hari!" touches the sky.

**94.** The sinful hearts cannot bear it:
the two brothers went out to the outer gate.

**95.** Their eyes red with anger, their faces crimson,
their clothes slipping again and again from their bodies.

**96.** Staggering, blind with anger, they go
and with threats and roars shout: "Stop, stop!"

**96+1.** Their eyes red, they shout in anger:
"We will destroy all the Vaishnavas in the town of Nadia!"[^30-12]

**97.** Standing in front, they look all around
and shout loudly: "Know your place — get out!

**98.** Hey you, wretched brahmana, Shani hangs over your life!" —
and with these words they hurl foul abuse.[^30-13]

**99.** Seeing their anger, the people of Nadia were frightened:
looking around, all were terrified.

**99+1.** Threatening and roaring, the brothers moved on,
while the devotees, raising their arms, cry: "Hari, Hari!"[^30-14]

**100.** Advaita Acharya Gosani and Nityananda,
Haridasa, Srinivasa, Murari, Mukunda —

**101.** and the Thakura Himself, Vishvambhara-raya,
together with His associates, sings Hari's qualities.

**101+1.** They sing with redoubled strength, increasing the jubilation:
the cry "Hari, Hari!" touches the sky[^30-15].

**102.** Happily, without weariness, they sing Hari's qualities,
while Jagai and Madhai in anger make mischief.

**102+1.** The two brothers cannot bear the name of Hari:
they rushed headlong to beat the devotees[^30-16].

**102+2.** Nityananda Raya, whose heart melts with compassion for the wretched,
looks at the two of them with eyes full of tears.

**102+3.** But even seeing those compassionate eyes the sinners did not soften;
then Nitai and Gaura stood before them.

**102+4.** At the sight [of Them] Jagai's heart melted:
Jagai stood rooted to the spot.

**103.** In anger both brothers run with sticks in their hands
and find before them a shard of a broken pot.

**104.** In anger they hurl that rim of the pot —
and it struck Nityananda's head with full force.

**105.** The shard struck with full force, blood flows in a stream;
seeing it, all [the Lord's] own cry out in dismay.[^30-17]

**105+1.** His head is split by the shard, but He takes no note of the pain:
crying "Gaura!", Nitai dances with a rejoicing heart[^30-18].

**105+2.** "You struck me with the rim of a pot — that I can bear,
but your evil plight I cannot bear.

**105+3.** You struck me — well and good, no harm in that;
only utter with your lips the sweetest name of Hari, brothers!"

**105+4.** Blood streams over all of Nityananda's body,
and Nityananda in supreme bliss gazes at Gauranga.

**105+5.** Overflowing with love, Mahaprabhu embraced Nitai
and wiped away the blood with His own cloth.

**106.** Seeing it, the Thakura was deeply grieved at heart
and called out, [standing] before those sinners:

**107.** "There are none more wicked than you two:
the earth spreads your names as the very name of sin.

**108.** You had done everything but one thing —
and now you have done that too; see it plainly!"[^30-19]

**109.** Saying this, Mahaprabhu went to Nityananda
and bound His head with His own cloth.

**110.** He knows the greatness of Nityananda Shripada:
lest His blood fall on the ground —

**111.** knowing that misfortune would then befall the earth —
the Lord, fearing this, bound His head with the cloth.[^30-20]

**111+1.** Then anger arose in Mahaprabhu,
and He called [His disc] to mind, crying: "Sudarshana-chakra!"[^30-21]

**111+2.** "Sudarshana!" — the Lord calls again and again;
hearing this, Murari Gupta gives a thunderous roar

**111+3.** and says: "Listen, Lord Vishvambhara:
give me the order — I will send these two to the abode of Yama!"

**111+4.** Hearing this, Nityananda holds Murari back by the hand —
and at that moment Sudarshana appeared in person.

**112.** In anger Gaurahari summons Sudarshana,
and Sudarshana stood [before Him] with joined palms:

**113.** "Why, O Ishvara, have You summoned me?
Glory, glory to Mahaprabhu, the son of Shachi!"

**114.** The Lord says: "Destroy Jagai and Madhai:
by striking Nityananda they have wounded My heart."

**115.** Hearing this, Sudarshana, terrible as the world's dissolution,
rushed towards Jagai and Madhai.

**116.** Jagai and Madhai saw Sudarshana:
their bodies began to tremble, their minds were seized with fear.

**117.** Seeing Sudarshana, Nityananda Prabhu laughs:
"What has Bhagavan done — what a great manifestation!"

**117+1.** My Nityananda Raya is an ocean of mercy:
"Do not kill!" He says, and holds Sudarshana back[^30-22].

**118.** "In Your compassion You have delivered the three worlds —
the poor and lowly, the fallen, the vile and the wicked.

**119.** Deliver Jagai and Madhai — be the Friend of the wretched,
keep the glory of the name 'Purifier of the fallen'."

**120.** Saying this, Nityananda fell at His feet
and spoke humbly at the Lord's feet:

**121.** "Lord, give these two fallen ones to me:
let the name 'Purifier of the fallen' be renowned.

**122.** In other ages You destroyed the demons —
deliver these two in their very bodies[^30-23].

**122+1.** With joined palms Nityananda says to the Lord:
"[Otherwise] there is no deliverance: the godless of the Kali age are untamable[^30-24].

**122+2.** You descended to begin sankirtana —
how [else] will You deliver the souls of the Kali age?"

**123.** Hearing Nityananda's words, the merciful Lord [says]:
"Blessed, blessed is Nityananda, the son of Rohini!"[^30-25]

**123+1.** The Lord says: "Nityananda, You are the Purifier of the fallen:
worshipping You, living beings obtain the treasure of prema[^30-26].

**123+2.** Through You will come the deliverance of the souls of Kali;
apart from You there is no [other] ocean of mercy.

**124.** All the scriptures say that I am under Your control:
what You have said I will surely do.

**125.** Whoever, having taken birth, even once says 'Nityananda'
is purified — that person is Mine."

**126.** Mahaprabhu went home with His own,
and Jagai and Madhai remained, astonished.

[^30-1]: The summary covers the whole section (vv. 1–169); this portion contains vv. 1–126, the rest is in the next. In the OCR of the edition many places are garbled or illegible (part of the summary ended up among the verses); what is restored by sense is in square brackets. Cf. Murari 2.10 and 2.13; Chaitanya-bhagavata, Madhya 13; Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 17.
[^30-2]: Vv. 2+1 — 2+7 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them in the main text. Cf. Murari 2.10.16–17, where this play is understood as a repetition of Krishna's play with the gopis' garments; here it is the Lord's playful sport with His intimates, in which He, "who knows every rasa", gives them joy.
