[^30-3]: Vv. 5–23 — cf. Murari 2.10.18–21. Nityananda's swaying gait, red eyes and loud cries are signs of His intoxication with prema (cf. en/28, note 12). The washing of His feet at the Lord's command teaches honour to the Vaishnava: the water of a devotee's feet purifies and bestows prema.
[^30-4]: In the 1983 edition this couplet stands after Advaita's arrival (vv. 29–30), and the honours and the meal refer to Him (cf. v. 34); in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition it comes before His arrival, so that they may also be referred to Haridasa (cf. Murari 2.10.4, where the Lord feeds Haridasa mahaprasada). We keep the order of the edition.
[^30-5]: According to Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition, Brahma and Prahlada are united in Haridasa Thakura (Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika); here Brahma manifests in him and praises the Lord with four mouths. Cf. Murari 2.10.24.
[^30-6]: Vv. 36–41 — the reasons for the Lord's descent: to taste His own bhava and to establish the dharma of the age, the sankirtana of the name (cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 3–4, where the inner reason is to taste Radha's love, and the outer one to spread the holy name and the prema of Vraja). Cf. Gita 4.8 on "destroying irreligion and establishing dharma".
[^30-7]: Vv. 43–46 — a list of associates; the presence of Narahari and Raghunandana, as above (en/29, note 7), is mentioned only by Lochana Dasa.
[^30-8]: "Tying it fast" — literally "making a knot (granthi)": perhaps counting the names on knots of a string is also meant.
[^30-9]: "Masters (thakuras) of Navadvipa" — according to the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya 13), Jagai and Madhai, brothers of a noble brahmana family, were the town's keepers of order (kotwals) and were sunk in vice.
[^30-10]: V. 62+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives it in a footnote as an additional reading of one printed book. Kayasthas are the caste of scribes. Cf. en/31, v. 148+1.
[^30-11]: The story of Ajamila is Bhagavata 6.1–2: the dying sinner called his son named Narayana, and even such an utterance of the name — namabhasa, the "shadow of the name", the name directed to another — saved him. Bhaktisiddhanta's summary stresses precisely this.
[^30-12]: V. 96+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it).
[^30-13]: Shani is Saturn, the planet-deity who brings misfortune; "Shani hangs over your life" is a threat: "you are done for".
[^30-14]: V. 99+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it).
[^30-15]: V. 101+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it); it nearly repeats v. 93.
[^30-16]: Vv. 102+1 — 102+4 are found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks them). In the 1983 edition v. 103 also differs: "Madhai runs in anger with a stick in his hand", so that Madhai alone throws the shard, while Jagai, touched by Nityananda's gaze, stands rooted to the spot (see note 17).
[^30-17]: In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition both brothers throw the shard. According to the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya 13), only Madhai struck Nityananda, while Jagai held him back from a second blow and was the first to receive mercy; the 1983 edition is close to this (vv. 102+1 — 102+4 and its reading of v. 103), and there, moreover, Nityananda, heedless of the pain, asks the brothers to sing the name of Hari (vv. 105+1 — 105+5). In Murari (2.13) the Lord promises to deliver Jagai and Madhai at Srivasa's request.
[^30-18]: Vv. 105+1 — 105+5 are found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks them). Nityananda grieves not over His wound but over the evil plight of the offenders — an image of His unparalleled mercy (see note 23).
[^30-19]: That is, they have offended a saint — Nityananda, a sannyasi and a Vaishnava; offence to a Vaishnava (vaishnava-aparadha) is the gravest of sins.
[^30-20]: Nityananda is Balarama, the Lord Himself (Sankarshana, the source of Ananta-Shesha); to spill His blood on the earth is a great offence that threatens the world with calamity.
[^30-21]: Vv. 111+1 — 111+4 are found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks them). Murari Gupta, according to Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition, is Hanuman (en/24, note 12) — hence his warlike roar.
[^30-22]: V. 117+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it); these are the author's words inserted into Nityananda's speech.
[^30-23]: The Lord's anger at the offenders of His devotee is also a manifestation of His love; and in the age of Kali the Lord takes up no weapons, but delivers by the holy name and prema (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 3, 17). Nityananda's intercession for His offenders is an image of His unparalleled mercy: according to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, Nityananda's grace makes no distinction between the worthy and the unworthy (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 5).
[^30-24]: Vv. 122+1 — 122+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks them).
[^30-25]: The son of Rohini is Balarama; thus Lochana Dasa directly calls Nityananda Balarama, as does the whole Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition.
[^30-26]: Vv. 123+1 — 123+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks them). In the 1983 edition v. 123 itself also differs: "Hearing Nityananda's words, Lord Gaurachandra began to weep, embracing Nityananda"; and the words "Blessed, blessed is Nityananda, the son of Rohini" stand there after v. 125 as the author's exclamation: "Blessed, blessed is Gaurachandra, the merciful Lord; blessed, blessed is Nityananda, the son of Rohini!" According to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, it is by Nityananda's mercy that one obtains Gaura's mercy (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 5, 8).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 8 (portion 31)

*The section "Pastimes with the Devotees and the Deliverance of Jagai and Madhai" (end): the repentance of Jagai and Madhai; the Lord takes their sins upon Himself and embraces them. The section "Wondrous Pastimes of Mahaprabhu in the Bhava of Bhagavan": the poor brahmana Vanamali and his son see Krishna in Vrindavana in the Lord — the "new Creator"; the bhava of Nrisimha in Srivasa's house; the singer of Shiva and the Lord in Shiva's bhava on his shoulders*

**127.** At the sight of Mahaprabhu and the sound of the sankirtana
they stand astonished, staring motionless [after Him].[^31-1]

**128.** Within themselves they reflect in their hearts,
pondering Mahaprabhu's words:

**129.** "We have committed a sin such as not even a beast would commit:
what not even it would do — we struck a sannyasi!"[^31-2]

**130.** Reflecting thus, they became pure in heart —
see, see the power of Mahaprabhu's mercy!

**131.** In the anguish of repentance both run with faces raised [to the sky],
and the people of Nadia, seeing it, were amazed.

**132.** Coming to Mahaprabhu's door,
they cry out wildly: "Thakura! Thakura!"

**133.** The Lord, sitting at home among His own, [says]:
"Who is calling Me? Look at the outer door.

**134.** Murari, bring them to Me at once."
Receiving the command, [Murari] embraced them both and brought them.

**135.** Seeing the Lord, with a cry of anguish
they fell at [His] feet on the ground — both brothers weep:

**136.** "You are the Purifier of the fallen, the ocean of mercy,
the Lord of all the worlds and above all the Friend of the wretched!"

**137.** The Lord is an ocean of mercy, His heart full of compassion:
seeing the suffering of the afflicted, He melts at once.

**138.** Raising them, He asked: "Listen, Jagai and Madhai,
why do you weep? Why have you come to Me?

**139.** You two are the foremost masters of Navadvipa,
clever men — why then do you weep now?"

**140.** Hearing this, Jagai and Madhai say:
"By Your mercy we have come to You.

**141.** How many sins we have committed — the killing of cows and women!
Beyond all count and measure — how many men we have killed!

**142.** Shame on our lordship over Nadia!
This body of ours [is stained] with the killing of gurus and the killing of brahmanas.

**143.** Brahmana women, Muslim women, the wives of gurus we did not leave alone;
chandala women and others — we spared no one.

**144.** To the people of the world we have done nothing but harm;
rites for the gods and the ancestors are not to our liking.

**145.** What more can we, worthless as we are, say to You?
There are not as many hairs on our heads as the sins we have committed.

**146.** All people speak of a sinner named Ajamila,
but he is no greater sinner than we," they said. —

**146+1.** "Out of love for his son he uttered the name 'Narayana',
and the brahmana, obtaining a divine body, went to Vaikuntha[^31-3].

**147.** The name 'Narayana' delivered him,
but [Narayana Himself], coming in person, cannot deliver us.[^31-4]

**148.** For us there is no deliverance — You Yourself know it:
for what merit will You show us mercy?

**148+1.** Even if a thousand scribes were to count for a hundred births,
still the sins of us two could not be counted"[^31-5].

**149.** Hearing such anguished words
and seeing that they were without pretence, the Thakura was filled with boundless compassion.

**150.** Seeing the suffering of the afflicted, the Thakura Himself suffers —
compassion embodied, oh, the very form of mercy!

**151.** The ocean of mercy reveals His mercy:
taking them by the hand, He led them to the Jahnavi.

**152.** The people of Nadia ran to see the wonder:
the Lord reveals prema — a sight unheard of!

**153.** The brahmanas and good people all stand and watch;
before them all the Lord speaks words of mercy:

**154.** "I shall take your sins upon Myself —
and you yourselves give Me your sins."

**155.** Saying this, He holds out His hand for the tulasi,[^31-6]
but the two brothers in fear do not give the tulasi.

**156.** In His mercy Gaura Bhagavan speaks again:
"Jagai and Madhai, give Me your sins as a gift!"

**157.** Jagai and Madhai say: "Listen, Lord:
our sins are so many that they cannot even be written down.

**158.** We are the lowest of the low, a storehouse of sins, sin itself;
to give You our sins — our hearts tremble with fear."

**159.** Hearing this, His eyes filled with tears,
and in a voice deep as thunder from a cloud He cried: "Hari bol!"

**160.** Again, asking for the gift of their sins, He holds out His hand —
and Jagai and Madhai placed the tulasi in His hand.

**161.** On all sides rose the cry: "Hari, Hari bol!"
The Lord embraced Jagai and Madhai.

**162.** He delivered the two brothers, Jagai and Madhai —
such sinners were granted the touch of the Lord!

**163.** Their voices choke with prema, their speech is broken,
their clothes are soaked with tears.

**164.** Their bodies thrill, their whole frames tremble;
falling at [the Lord's] feet on the ground, they speak in anguish:

**165.** "Is there anywhere another such Thakura —
an ocean of mercy, the great Purifier of the fallen!"

**166.** He delivers such sinners as Jagai and Madhai:
by the touch of His divine body they dance, overflowing with prema.

**167.** Having taken upon Himself the sins of Jagai and Madhai,
the Lord Vishvambhara Hari Himself dances.[^31-7]

**168.** Where else is such a Thakura, a treasury of mercy,
who sees no fault and loves so boundlessly?

**169.** He delivers souls and dances with joy —
on this Lochana Dasa firmly sets his hope.

## Wondrous Pastimes of Mahaprabhu in the Bhava of Bhagavan

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One day, while Sriman Mahaprabhu was joyfully sporting with the devotees, a poor brahmana named Vanamali, a native of East Bengal, came there with his son, and Sriman Mahaprabhu cast a merciful glance upon them. At this they suddenly became mad with the bliss of prema, and when the sankirtana began, they saw Sriman Mahaprabhu right there in the form of Shyamasundara and fainted with supreme bliss; on regaining consciousness they praised Sriman Mahaprabhu, the Giver of prema, and called Him the "new Creator", since, freeing [people] from Vedic rites, He bestows prema on all living beings. Then one day in Srivasa's house, while listening to the hymn "The Thousand Names of Vishnu", [the Lord] suddenly roared in the bhava of Nrisimha; and when everyone in fear began to flee in all directions, He restrained His bhava of Nrisimha. On another day, when a worshipper of Shiva began to sing of Shiva's qualities, Gaurasundara, greatly pleased at hearing the qualities of the [great] devotee Shiva sung, climbed onto his shoulders and danced in the bhava of Shiva. On yet another day, when a brahmana woman touched Sriman Mahaprabhu's feet, Sriman Mahaprabhu, deeply grieved, leapt into the Ganga; the devotees drew Him out onto the bank and satisfied Sriman Mahaprabhu with all kinds of prayers and praises. Then Sriman Mahaprabhu taught that everyone who has obtained the rare human body must serve Hari, and that without serving Hari there is no meaning in human birth; [then are described] the embrace [of Murari], [his] humble words, the manifestation [by the Lord] of His divine form, the bathing [of the Lord] with Ganga water by Srivasa Pandita, the cleaning of the temple together with the devotees headed by Advaita Acharya, and so on; [at the end] the author, singing Gaura's qualities, instructs everyone to serve Gaura.[^31-8]

*Raga dhanashi; refrain: "O Lord, Dvijachand! To deliver the world He lays many snares"*

**1.** Glory, glory to Gadadhara, Gauranga and Narahari!
Whoever hears of Gauranga's qualities obtains prema.

**2.** Hear another wondrous story of another day:
in Navadvipa the supreme, great treasure was revealed.

**3.** The Lord stays at home with His own
and softly lets fall words from His lotus mouth.

**4.** A stream of a river of nectar flows unceasingly;
bathed in it, the devotees are manifestly intoxicated.

**5.** Thus the Lord abides in joyful play —
and suddenly a beggar came there,

**6.** named Vanamali, and with him his son;
born in a brahmana family, he lives in the east, in Banga.[^31-9]

**6+1.** Scorched by the fire of poverty, he had come to these parts,
and, seeing Gaurachandra, the brahmana was filled with gladness[^31-10].

**7.** He saw Vishvambhara surrounded by devotees —
and the brahmana and his son were filled with joy.

**8.** The brahmana and his son reflect [within];
he cannot speak — his voice chokes in his throat.

**9.** "It is well that I became poor:
because of poverty I came [here] — and was purified.

**10.** I know for certain: Vishvambhara is Bhagavan;
I have realized in my heart that He is no one else.

**11.** Today, I feel, my birth has been fulfilled:
I have seen Vishvambhara, the golden one, a mine of virtues.

**12.** As soon as I saw Him, my eyes and heart were soothed,
the wretched, unbearable fire of poverty was quenched.

**13.** As the heart is content on tasting nectar,
so at the sight of Gaurachandra my whole body is [as if] bathed."

**14.** Then Gaura Bhagavan, seeing him,
looked with merciful eyes upon the two brahmanas.

**15.** Joyfully He sings of Hari's qualities with the two,
and by the Lord's grace they obtained the treasure of prema.

**16.** In joy the brahmana dances, his son dances;
in a moment the thread of samsara was cut for him.

**17.** Such is Mahaprabhu Gora, the ocean of mercy:
there is no greater Friend of the wretched than He.

**18.** The next day, in the midst of the sankirtana,
the Thakura Vishvambhara, king of dancers, dances.

**19.** Then those two brahmanas suddenly
saw a certain boy — and their hearts were startled:

**20.** the Lord in His golden body had become dark,
a yellow cloth shines on His hips, in His hand a splendid flute,

**21.** a crest of peacock feathers flutters in the wind.
[Seeing] that form, all [His] companions sing.

**22.** In Vrindavana, in the depths of the forest, beside Radha,
they saw the Dark One, the king of dancers.

**23.** There too they see the Yamuna, Mount Govardhana,
Bahula, Bhandira, Madhuvana and the other [forests];

**24.** they see the cows, the gopis, the cowherd boys and Talavana —
in Navadvipa they saw Madana-Gopala.[^31-11]

**25.** Seeing this, the brahmana fell unconscious:
his body thrilling, his eyes in tears.

**26.** Again and again he cries "hum!", slaps himself like a wrestler,
shouts "Here is Krishna, Krishna!" — and a crowd gathered round.[^31-12]

**27.** Seeing this, the Thakura again stopped dancing
and, crying "Hold him, hold him!", again caught hold of the brahmana.

**28.** Hear, all of you, this song of Gora's qualities:
this new Creator reveals mercy.

**29.** Removing the bonds of karma, He gives the treasure of prema —
where else is there such a Thakura?

**30.** Beyond samsara He creates His own world —
prema-bhakti directed to Himself, beyond the objects of the senses.[^31-13]

**31.** Every day He wears divine garlands, sandal paste, prasada,
[yet] there is no attachment in Him — only love for all.

**32.** Unattached, He cannot live without the company [of devotees];
beyond karma, He performs acts according to injunction.

**33.** All that is proper according to the Vedic injunctions
He performs in full — yet in effect the result is the opposite.

**34.** Thus He reveals His treasure of prema-bhakti;
therefore I call Him: "The new Creator, a jewel."

**35.** Such is my Gora-raya, the ocean of mercy:
all without effort obtain the supreme treasure.

**36.** There is no other such Thakura, the Giver of prema —
says Lochana: worship the new Creator!

*Refrain: "Whoever has once seen Gora's beauty cannot forget it — he pines away all his life"*

**37.** And then one more day — hear of a wonder —
a joyful festival in the house of Srivasa Pandita.

**38.** That Srivasa Pandita performs the rite for the ancestors
and with a most pure heart listens to the "Thousand Names".

**39.** At that time Gaurahari came there
and, fulfilling His heart's desire, listens to the "Thousand Names".

**40.** As He listened, the bhava of Nrisimha arose in Him:[^31-14]
His two eyes reddened with anger, His hair stood on end.

**41.** His whole body thrilling, His colour crimson,
again and again a fierce "hum!", a lion's roar.

**42.** Suddenly He seized a club and rushed [forward] —
seeing this, all the people trembled at heart.

**43.** Everyone flees without even tying up their hair:
they cannot bear the Lord's wrathful bhava.

**44.** Seeing the people take to flight, Nrihari[^31-15]
in a moment threw down the club, restraining His bhava.

**45.** The son of Shachi is the seed of all avataras:
whoever comes to His mind, that He becomes.[^31-16]

**46.** Restraining His bhava, the Lord sat down on His seat
and, astonished, spoke:

**47.** "I do not know — have I committed some offence?
Or is something on your minds?"

**48.** Hearing this, all said:
"What offence of Yours? What are You saying?"

**49.** Srivasa said: "Whoever has seen You [thus]
has been released from all bonds."

**50.** The next day — hear this story, all of you —
there suddenly came a singer who sang of Shiva.

**51.** Bowing at the feet of Gaurahari,
he sings of Mahesha's qualities with a joyful heart.

**52.** In great ecstasy he calls out: "Shiva! Shiva!" —
devotion to Shiva shows itself in his body.

**53.** Hearing him, the Thakura rejoiced at heart:
at the song of Shiva's qualities His happiness grew abundantly.

**54.** Then, in the bhava of Shiva, the dancing began;
the singer of Shiva forgets himself in happiness.

**55.** There is no one more fortunate than he:
the Thakura Himself climbed onto his shoulders.

**56.** Carrying [the Lord] on his shoulders, the singer dances in joy,
and the Lord's eyes turned red in His bhava.

**57.** In the bhava of Shiva He speaks Shiva's words:
the damaru rattles, with His mouth He sounds a horn.

**58.** "Rama! Krishna!" He calls, weeping and laughing;
now Gora weeps in the bhava of Shiva.[^31-17]

**59.** Srivasa Pandita knows all this truth:
with attentive mind he recites a hymn to Shiva.

**60.** Sri Mukunda Datta recites the "Mahimna-stava";
they dance in joy — they know the whole truth.

**61.** The Thakura came down from the singer's shoulders
and, devoted to Hari, sings abundantly of Hari.

**62.** He dances in joy, as if drunk with wine,
and happily sings Hari's qualities — an ocean of bliss.

**63.** The ocean of mercy reveals mercy —
hearing this, Lochana Dasa is lost in joy.

[^31-1]: Vv. 127–169 are the end of the section (Bhaktisiddhanta's summary is at its beginning, en/30). Cf. Chaitanya-bhagavata, Madhya 13; Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 17. Murari (2.13) has only the Lord's promise to deliver Jagai and Madhai. In the 1983 edition the second line reads: "purified [in heart], they stand motionless".
[^31-2]: The sannyasi is Nityananda, the avadhuta. The first line in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition is not entirely clear; we read "মৃগে", "a beast". The 1983 edition has: "There is no sin we have not committed; and what we had not committed — [we have committed now]: we struck a sannyasi."
[^31-3]: V. 146+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it); cf. en/30, v. 67.
[^31-4]: On Ajamila see en/30, note 11.
[^31-5]: V. 148+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it); cf. en/30, v. 62+1.
[^31-6]: A ritual gift (dana) is handed over by placing a tulasi leaf in the recipient's hand; the Lord asks the brothers to give Him their sins as a ritual gift.
[^31-7]: Sin cannot touch the Lord: "the powerful are not stained [by what stains others], like the all-consuming fire" (Bhagavata 10.33.29–30). In accepting the sins of Jagai and Madhai the Lord shows His causeless mercy; cf. Chaitanya-bhagavata, Madhya 13, where the Lord likewise takes all their sins upon Himself, on condition that they sin no more.
[^31-8]: The summary covers the whole section (vv. 1–135); this portion contains vv. 1–63, the rest is in the next. The OCR of the summary is garbled in places; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface (there: "the Lord's teaching on serving Hari, mercy to Murari, Murari's praise, the Lord's manifestation of His divine form, the bathing [of the Lord] by Srivasa, the author's singing of Gaura's qualities and his instruction to serve Gaura"). "The bhava of Bhagavan" — the Lord reveals His own divinity and His own forms (Krishna, Nrisimha and others); this is not "possession" by some other being (cf. v. 45). Cf. Murari 2.11–2.13.
[^31-9]: This is not Vanamali Acharya, the matchmaker of the Lord's first marriage (en/17). Banga is East Bengal.
[^31-10]: V. 6+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it).
[^31-11]: Bahula, Bhandira, Madhuvana and Talavana are forests of Vraja; Madana-Gopala is Krishna who enchants even the god of love. In the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding Gaura is Krishna Himself, who has covered His dark complexion with Radha's golden radiance (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 4); here for a moment He reveals His original form to the brahmanas. Cf. Murari 2.11.3–6. The 1983 edition has "and their retinue (avarana)" instead of "and Talavana".
[^31-12]: Literally "he set up a market" — a Bengali idiom: he raised such a commotion that people came running.
[^31-13]: Vv. 28–36 explain why the Lord is called the "new Creator" (navina vidhata): the old Creator, Brahma, made the world of karma and rites, while Gaura, removing the bonds of karma, "creates His own world" — prema directed to Himself. Bhaktisiddhanta explains in his summary that, freeing [people] from Vedic rites, He bestows prema on all. This is no rejection of the Vedas: the highest aim of the Vedas is bhakti; the Lord Himself follows the injunctions to set an example, but gives what rites cannot give (vv. 32–33).
[^31-14]: Nrisimha (Narasimha) is the Man-Lion, the avatara who appeared for Prahlada's sake. The bhava of Nrisimha is the Lord's own, not an alien "possession": the Lord is the source of all avataras (v. 45). The "Thousand Names" is the Vishnu-sahasranama. Cf. Murari 2.11.6–12.
[^31-15]: The text has "Narahari" ("Man-Hari", i.e. Nrisimha) — here the Lord Himself in that bhava, not Narahari Sarakara. Cf. Murari 2.11.9 ("Nrihari").
[^31-16]: Krishna is the source of all avataras (Bhagavata 1.3.28; Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 2), and Gaura is Krishna Himself; therefore the forms of Nrisimha, Varaha and others that Gaura reveals are His own.
[^31-17]: Shiva is the greatest of Vaishnavas (Bhagavata 12.13.16). Rejoicing in the song about His devotee, the Lord takes on his bhava and, like Shiva, who eternally worships Rama, calls: "Rama! Krishna!" The damaru and the horn are Shiva's emblems; the "Mahimna-stava" is Pushpadanta's Shiva-mahimna-stotra. Cf. Murari 2.11.13–19.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 9 (portion 32)

*The section "Wondrous Pastimes of Mahaprabhu in the Bhava of Bhagavan" (end): a brahmana takes the dust of the Lord's feet, and the Lord leaps into the Ganga; Nityananda brings Him out onto the bank; the Lord goes off to the northern bank and returns at the devotees' entreaties; in Srivasa's house — words on serving Krishna and on His departure; Murari, embraced by the Lord, recites the verse of Sudama; the Lord reveals His radiance, and Srivasa bathes Him; the Lord and the devotees clean the temple; Lochana Dasa calls on all to serve Gaura. The section "The Leper's Release from Sin and the Bhava of Baladeva": a leper who had offended Srivasa begs the Lord for deliverance; the Lord rejects him and delivers him only when Srivasa forgives*

*Refrain: "Who does not weep at the qualities of my Gauranga? He has bound the minds of all living beings with prema"*

**64.** Hear one more wonder — on the next day,
when the dancing with His own had ended,

**65.** the Lord fell flat on the ground like a rod,
and all the people in joy cry: "Hari, Hari!"

**66.** At that very moment a certain brahmana came up[^32-1]
and, smiling, took the dust of the Lord's lotus feet.

**67.** Seeing this, Gaura Bhagavan quickly rose:
seeing the brahmana's deed, He was grieved.

**68.** In great remorse, His face downcast,
in displeasure He breathes heavily and fast.

**69.** Quickly rising, the Lord suddenly ran
and swiftly leapt into the waters of the Jahnavi.

**70.** The Lord sank under the water — He cannot be seen;
all His own leapt in after Him.

**71.** All the people of Nadia thought a calamity had happened:
all weep, all are in sorrow.

**72.** Crying "My son! My son!" runs Shachi, His mother,
wanting to leap in where Vishvambhara Hari is.

**73.** Shachi, distraught, wails aloud,
weeps and sobs, rolls on the ground.

**74.** Seeing such calamity, the Avadhuta-raya
leapt into the Ganga in search of the Lord.

**75.** Diving into the water, He caught the Lord by the hand
and, holding Him, suddenly brought Him out onto the bank of the Ganga.

**76.** Seeing this, all the people were overjoyed;
all [the Lord's] own, coming to their senses, weep.

**77.** Shachi-devi weeps, holding Vishvambhara in her arms;
[weep] Srivasa, Murari, Mukunda, Shuklambara,

**77+1.** Gadadhara and Narahari weep, clasping [His] feet,
Vasudeva and Jagadananda weep, gazing at [His] face[^32-2],

**78.** Haridasa and all, all His own —
they weep as they look at Gaura's face, still shaken with fear.

**79.** All the other people, who had grieved greatly,
seeing Gaura's face, went home happy.

**80.** Then the Lord Vishvambhara, with everyone,
quickly went to the house of Murari Gupta.

**81.** After staying there a little, the Lord swiftly left
and unexpectedly came to the house of Vijaya Mishra.[^32-3]

**82.** Having spent the night, the Lord rose at dawn
and unexpectedly went to the northern bank of the Ganga.

**83.** He wanders there — His mind is past understanding;
all who were with Him were frightened.

**84.** The brahmanas, the good people and all His own
together made a humble plea to Him:

**85.** "Be gracious, Lord Gaura, treasury of virtues!" —
so all these offenders say in anguish. —

**86.** "Have mercy, Mahaprabhu, give up Your great anger:
how many such faults of Your servants will You hold against them?

**87.** The Lord is an ocean of mercy, mercy embodied;
You descended out of mercy, in grace towards the people.

**88.** Why have You Yourself so turned away [from us]?
How can we know what is in Your heart, what Your ways are?

**89.** Come home, Lord, avert this calamity;
look upon Your followers — be gracious!"

**90.** When His own thus humbly pleaded,
the Lord's compassionate heart then melted.

**91.** The Lord returned home with a joyful heart
and with His followers sings of His own qualities.

**92.** In the town of Nadia there arose jubilation —
Lochana Dasa happily sings of Gora's qualities.

*Raga varadi; disha: "Haya re haya, are haya!" (murchha); refrain: "I take upon myself all misfortunes of Gora's beauty! He has given away the treasure of prema, filling the world with it"*

**93.** Leaving grief behind, with a joyful heart Gaurahari
went with His own to Srivasa's house.

**94.** Srinivasa, Haridasa and all the others
sit near the Thakura and gaze at His face.

**95.** Then Mahaprabhu, before the whole assembly,
speaks words from His heart, and all listen:

**96.** "Wealth, kin, youth — all is in vain:
I have not served the true treasure — the feet of Krishna.

**97.** My heart burns ceaselessly in samsara:
having obtained such a body, I did no work for Krishna.

**98.** In this world the human body is rare;
and for serving Krishna, what does it matter — man or woman?

**99.** If one does not serve Krishna, this whole body is in vain,
husband, son, father, mother — all is in vain, and the home.

**100.** Leaving My mother, I shall go to distant lands" —
so He revealed to all His heart's word.[^32-4]

**101.** "All the people say that I act wrongly."
Murari says: "To hear this is death to me.

**102.** No one says so, listen, Mahaprabhu:
we have never heard it from anyone's lips."

**103.** Hearing this, Gaura Bhagavan
embraced Murari.

**104.** Embracing Murari, He went [with him] into the house;
in the Lord's embrace the vaidya forgot himself.

**105.** His whole body thrilled from head to foot;
he recited an ancient verse:

**106.** "Where am I — a poor man, most sinful — and where is Krishna, the abode of Shri!
Yet me, a brahmana only by birth, He embraced with His arms."[^32-5]

**107.** Hearing this, He revealed His lordship —
a radiance surpassing the rays of millions of suns.

**108.** Sitting on His seat, He speaks sweet words:
"Here I am — consciousness and bliss; do not think I am far away."[^32-6]

**109.** Hearing this, all were overwhelmed with bliss,
the bodies of all were filled with thrill.

**110.** Srivasa Pandita, excellent in conduct,
bathes Him with the water of the Ganga.

**111.** He performed the bathing and worship according to rule,
and the Treasury of virtues was pleased with his worship.

**112.** In joy all the people sing of Hari's qualities;
looking at the faces of the devotees, Gora-raya dances.

**113.** Placing on his head the lotus feet of Narahari,
Lochana Dasa speaks of the sweetness of Gauranga.

**114.** Hear an unheard-of story of the following day:
listen attentively — I shall tell it now.

**115.** The Teacher of the world teaches all people —
the ocean of mercy, the wish-fulfilling tree of prema-bhakti.

**116.** All He does is to enlighten His own.
Taking with Him Advaita Acharya and the others —

**117.** Srinivasa, Haridasa, Murari, Mukunda,
Gadadhara, Shuklambara, Rama and the rest,

**118.** Narahari, Raghunandana, Sri Mukunda Dasa,
Vasu Ghosha, Jagadananda and all the servants —

**119.** taking all the devotees with Him,
the Lord joyfully goes to the temple.[^32-7]

**120.** He wears a loincloth, a hoe on His shoulder,
a broom in His hand, and all are together with Him.

**121.** All His companions wear the same dress:
brooms in their hands, hoes on their shoulders, their hair tied up high.

**122.** The Lord goes to clean the temple —
never have I heard such a wondrous story.

**123.** Becoming Krishna's sweeper, He goes from door to door;
all the Vaishnavas together sweep.[^32-8]

**124.** Thus the Thakura teaches the people:
serve [Him], all people, whoever is wise!

**125.** There is no other Giver of prema-bhakti —
knowing this, serve the feet of Sri Gauranga.

**126.** In age after age come so many avataras —
and they return service for service in like measure.

**127.** No one else has shown such lordship:
to teach bhakti He puts a hoe on His shoulder.

**128.** In what age was there one who serves those who do not serve Him,
who goes from house to house begging [them to accept] devotion to Himself?

**129.** "He who returns service for service is already a great lord" —
so say the devotees; and others say [God] is far away.[^32-9]

**130.** Without considering who is worthy or unworthy, in any land,
He gives all the treasure of Vrindavana and gladdens all.

**131.** Prema beyond dharma and adharma He offers to all —
the son of Shachi, the Lord, has delivered all.

**132.** Brahma, Maheshvara, Lakshmi and Ananta
cannot call it their own — so hard is it to obtain.

**133.** There is no [other] lord who calls his own those who do not serve him —
this is why my heart longs for Gora's qualities.

**133+1.** Mighty is the remembrance of the glories of Gauranga's feet:
this power alone carries everyone across samsara[^32-10].

**134.** Brothers, serve Gora's qualities, do not neglect them:
to cross samsara, this alone is the raft.

**135.** There will never be such a Thakura again —
says Lochana: Gora's avatara alone.

## The Leper's Release from Sin and the Bhava of Baladeva

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* One day, when Sriman Mahaprabhu was walking with the devotees, absorbed in prema, a brahmana afflicted with leprosy on that road appealed to Sriman Mahaprabhu for his deliverance. Sriman Mahaprabhu at first rejected him as an offender against a Vaishnava, then told him to beg Srivasa Pandita's forgiveness for his offence, and at last, freeing him from the offence against the Vaishnava, bestowed prema on him. A certain brahmana wished to see Gaurasundara's dancing, but the devotees of Gaura headed by Srivasa did not admit him, and his desire was not fulfilled; so one day, seeing Sriman Mahaprabhu bathing in the Ganga, he angrily cursed Him: "May Your worldly happiness be destroyed!" Sriman Mahaprabhu accepted the brahmana's curse with joy. Then the brahmana came to his senses and in fear began to praise Sriman Mahaprabhu, and Sriman Mahaprabhu consoled him, revealing that the brahmana's curse was what He Himself desired. Then are described wondrous pastimes: Sriman Mahaprabhu in the bhava of Balarama cries out "Give Me honey!"; with the devotees, performing kirtana, He goes to the house of Sri Advaita Acharya; the next day, when He has fainted in the bhava of Baladeva, Gadadhara comes and He restrains His bhava; the devotees headed by Acharyaratna arrive; Srivasa and the other devotees see Sriman Mahaprabhu in the form of Baladeva; He goes with the devotees to bathe in the Ganga, and so on.[^32-11]

*Refrain: "Hari, Rama, Narayana — Shachi's darling, golden Gora!"*

**1.** Hear another wondrous story of Gauranga's deeds:
hearing it, you will gain great love.

**2.** The Lord walks along the road with His own;
from the taste of talk of Krishna His body sways in rapture.

**3.** On that road there was a leper;
humbly he addresses the Lord's feet.

**4.** Falling to the ground, he bows down
and in anguish humbly speaks:

**5.** "All people say, Lord: You are Janardana,
You are Purushottama, You are the Eternal.

**6.** You are the Lord of the lords of the gods, the Friend of the three worlds;
deliver me, O ocean of mercy!

**7.** Hearing that You are the Purifier of the fallen, I have come to You:
deliver me — You are the Master of all.

**8.** O Lord of the destitute, Shachi's darling,
deliver me, Lord Gauranga-Gopala!

**9.** In the three worlds there is no greater sinner than I;
free [me] from this unbearable leprosy."

**10.** Hearing this, the Lord grew angry at heart
and looked wrathfully at the leper.

**11.** The Thakura says: "Listen, sinful wretch:
why did you, worthless one, revile a Vaishnava?

**12.** All living beings in the world are My friends;
whoever hates a Vaishnava is My enemy.

**13.** At blasphemy against Myself I am never grieved,
but how can I be happy when Srivasa Pandita is reviled?

**14.** You spoke unspeakable words to him:
even if you suffer a hundred births, [the sin] will not leave you.

**15.** Whoever offends a Vaishnava,
him I never deliver.

**16.** See: this body of Mine is [My] life outside,
and the Vaishnava is [My] life within — there is no doubt.[^32-12]

**17.** Whoever serves the Vaishnava, even if he hates Me,
him I deliver, removing his distress.

**18.** The fool who does harm to a Vaishnava
falls into hell, and he has no refuge.

**19.** You are a sinner, the greatest scoundrel, incorrigible:
how long you will suffer in hell — there is no end!"

**20.** Hearing this, the leper falls down and weeps,
weeps in distress and finds no peace:

**21.** "In other avataras You were merciful, discerning who was a devotee —
but now, Lord, in Kali [You deliver] scoundrels in every home.

**22.** If You strike down those who do not serve You,
how will You bear the name of Purifier of the fallen?

**23.** Glory to You whose name, Vishvambhara, is the welfare of all!
Glory to You, Mighty-armed, the support of the bridge of dharma!

**24.** By Your bridge people cross the ocean of existence —
do not cast me away, Lord, son of Shachi!"

**25.** Though, seeing him, [the Lord] had softened at heart,
yet He is subject to the Vaishnavas — not independent.[^32-13]

**26.** Knowing this, the Lord went to Srivasa's house
and, sitting down, the Great Soul tells him everything:

**27.** "On the road I saw a leper:
for his offence he will suffer many births.

**28.** For his offence against you his whole body is rotting,
and seeing him, I felt no pity for him.

**29.** 'Deliver me!' the leper cries;
but who will deliver one who has offended you?

**30.** Only if you yourself look on him with kindness
will the sinner be delivered by your mercy."

**31.** Hearing this, Srivasa Pandita
began to laugh, hearing of the Lord's conduct:

**32.** "I am the lowest of the low — and You say such things to me!
Using me as a pretext, You deliver the sinner.

**33.** His fault towards me is entirely gone;
I am pleased — relieve him of his suffering."

**34.** Hearing this, the Lord cried aloud "Hari!" —
and the leper was delivered: such [was] the grace.

**35.** There, on the bank of the Ganga, that very moment the leper
received the supreme medicine — the mercy of Srivasa.[^32-14]

**36.** That very moment his body became divine;
crying "Gauranga!", he runs, overcome with longing:

**36+1.** Intoxicated with great prema, he gives thunderous cries:
now he swoons, now he raves without end[^32-15].

**37.** "Where has Gaurachandra gone, the moon of my heart?
Who else so frees one from the great blindness — the disease of existence?"

**38.** Meanwhile Gaurachandra left Srinivasa's house
and hurried to see the leper.

**39.** On the road they met,
and [he], clasping the Lord's feet, fell to the ground.

**40.** Raising him, the Lord embraced him
and that very moment gave him prema, hard even for Brahma to obtain.

**41.** He laughs, weeps, dances, sings, rolls on the ground,
whirls about in dance, crying: "Friend of Gadadhara!"[^32-16]

**42.** Seeing this, all the devotees rejoiced,
and all Nadia, seeing it, was amazed.

**43.** Hear, all of you, of Vishvambhara's deeds:
hearing of them, you will soon obtain prema-bhakti.

**43+1.** Then Mahaprabhu, rejoicing at heart,
dances — and prema became manifest in the brahmana's body[^32-17].

**43+2.** Seeing this, Mahaprabhu loudly cries "Hari!":
He delivered the leper, He showed him grace.

**44.** Most wondrous is this manifestation in Nadia —
hearing of it, Lochana Dasa is lost in joy.

[^32-1]: The text has "a brahmana" (so also the 1983 edition); in Bhaktisiddhanta's summary (en/31, at the head of the section "Wondrous Pastimes of Mahaprabhu in the Bhava of Bhagavan") and in Murari (2.11.21) it is a brahmana woman. The Lord, playing the role of a devotee, grieves at having received an honour befitting the Lord. Vv. 64–79 — cf. Murari 2.11.19–25.
[^32-2]: V. 77+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives it in a footnote as an additional reading (in another form: "Gadadhara and Narahari weep, holding the Lord; Vasudeva and Jagadananda weep, gazing at the Lord").
[^32-3]: Vv. 80–92 — cf. Murari 2.12.1–4. Vijaya is probably Vijaya Dasa, a devotee of Navadvipa and copyist of books.
[^32-4]: The Lord is already thinking of sannyasa. Vv. 93–102 — cf. Murari 2.12.5–8.
[^32-5]: Bhagavata 10.81.16 — the words of the poor brahmana Sudama whom Krishna embraced; Murari applies them to himself. In the edition the verse is followed by Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya and translation: "Where am I, a sinful pauper, and where is Krishnachandra, full of all opulence! Only because I am the lowest of brahmanas did Bhagavan embrace me with both arms." Cf. Murari 2.12.9–11.
[^32-6]: Literally "I am Chidananda": the Lord's body is not material — it is consciousness and bliss (cf. Murari 2.12.13: "this body is the highest concentration of being and consciousness"). Vv. 107–111 — cf. Murari 2.12.12–15.
[^32-7]: Vv. 114–123 — cf. Murari 2.13 (the Lord and the devotees clean the temple). Narahari, Raghunandana and Mukunda Dasa in this list appear only in Lochana Dasa (cf. en/30, note 7); "Rama" is Sri Rama Pandita, Srivasa's brother.
[^32-8]: A haddipa is a sweeper of the lowest caste of scavengers. The Lord Himself takes on the lowliest service, teaching humility and service to the Deity; cf. the cleaning of the Gundicha temple (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 12).
[^32-9]: Vv. 126–133: the other avataras return service for service (cf. Gita 4.11: "as they surrender to Me, so I reciprocate"), while Gaura Himself goes from house to house offering prema even to those who do not serve Him.
[^32-10]: V. 133+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it).
[^32-11]: The summary covers both this section (the leper, vv. 1–44; the brahmana's curse, vv. 45–69) and the pastimes in the bhava of Balarama, which in the text of the edition already form the next section. The OCR of the summary is garbled in places; gaps are filled from the table of contents in the edition's preface. Cf. Murari 2.13; Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 17. In the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Adi 17) this is the brahmana Gopala Chapala, who slandered Srivasa and was struck with leprosy; the Lord refused him and later told him to beg Srivasa's forgiveness.
[^32-12]: Vv. 11–19 speak of the gravity of offence against a Vaishnava (vaishnava-aparadha): the Lord does not forgive it Himself until the offended Vaishnava forgives. The words "the Vaishnava is My life within" convey the teaching that the devotee is dearer to the Lord than His own body (cf. Bhagavata 9.4.68: "the sadhus are My heart, and I am the heart of the sadhus").
[^32-13]: The Lord is the independent Master, but of His own will He submits to the love of His devotees (bhakta-adhina); therefore He places the Vaishnava's forgiveness above His own.
[^32-14]: In the 1983 edition this story is given in a different redaction (the 1903 edition agrees with Bhaktisiddhanta; we follow Bhaktisiddhanta's edition). Instead of vv. 20–24 it has: "Hearing this, the brahmana was filled with anguish and, falling to the ground, began to plead piteously: 'Glory, glory to Mahaprabhu, be merciful to me! The Vedas call You the Purifier of the fallen. If You bear the name Purifier of the fallen, You will surely deliver me. How many great sinners You have delivered — deliver me too, O lotus-eyed One! There is no sinner in the three worlds equal to me; I am suffering — free [me] from leprosy.'" V. 25 there reads: "Then compassion awoke in the Lord's heart, yet He is subject to the Vaishnavas, not independent." Instead of v. 34: "The Lord says: 'Srinivasa, listen to Me: take everyone and go to where the leper is.' Then all went there together joyfully and sprinkled him with the water of Srivasa's feet. As soon as the drops of that water touched his body, his body shone like gold and the disease vanished. Then his heart was filled with great bliss, and he joyfully began to dance, crying 'Hari, Hari!'" From v. 35 on the two redactions agree. The sense is the same: an offence against a Vaishnava is removed only by the mercy of the offended Vaishnava himself (the water of his feet).
[^32-15]: V. 36+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks it).
[^32-16]: "Friend of Gadadhara" — a name of Gaura, inseparable from Gadadhara Pandita (in the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding, Radha; en/25, note 7).
[^32-17]: Vv. 43+1 — 43+2 are found only in the 1983 edition (the 1903 edition lacks them).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 10 (portion 33)

*The section "The Leper's Release from Sin and the Bhava of Baladeva" (end): a brahmana who was not admitted to see the Lord's dancing curses Him — "Be outside samsara!" — and the Lord accepts the curse as a blessing. The section "Shachi's Grief over the Brahmana's Curse" (only in the 1983 edition): Shachi in despair, the Lord consoles her. The section "Mahaprabhu Distributes Prema in His Bhavas": the Lord in the bhava of Balarama demands honey; the vision of Halayudha and His words to Gadadhara; the fragrance of varuni and Balarama's divine companions; the Lord tells of Varaha and Halayudha and asks for His flute*

**45.** Then on another day the Lord was dancing,
and at the door stood a brahmana.

**46.** At that very time another brahmana came:
he wished to see Gaurachandra dance.

**47.** The one who stood at the door did not let him in,
and the brahmana was grieved at not being able to see.[^33-1]

**48.** Grieved, the brahmana went home;
the Lord danced in joy and knew nothing of it.

**49.** The next day, as the Lord was bathing in the Ganga,
that brahmana suddenly saw the Lord.

**50.** He saw the Lord Vishvambhara bathing in the Ganga —
and the brahmana glares in anger, his whole body trembling.

**51.** Seeing the Lord, he speaks angry words:
"I came to Your house wishing to see You.

**52.** I greatly longed to see Your dancing,
but a sinful brahmana barred the way.

**53.** He did not let me in, [kept me] outside the door —
so You too shall be outside samsara!"

**54.** Saying this, he tore his sacred thread in anger;
beside himself with rage, the brahmana heeds no persuasion.[^33-2]

**55.** "I was put outside the door — I will not bear it!" —
and he cursed: "May You be outside samsara!"

**56.** Hearing this, the Lord rejoiced at heart:
"The brahmana's curse has become a great blessing for Me!"

**57.** When Bhagavan accepted the curse,
the brahmana, hearing it, grew greatly afraid at heart:

**58.** "What could I do, Lord? It is You who made me say it:
You are all-perfect, the Inner Witness of all.

**59.** You will deliver all who are given to idle argument:
taking sannyasa, You will give them prema.

**60.** Seeing You a sannyasi, they will call You 'guru',
and through that humility You will give them prema.

**61.** Gaurahari, the crest-jewel of the wise,
will open the storehouse of the prema of former times and distribute it.

**62.** You have vowed to flood the universe [with prema] —
You will leave out no one, neither the wicked nor the good.

**63.** I alone have been left out of the flood of Your prema:
what will become of me, O Purifier of the fallen?"[^33-3]

**64.** Hearing this, the Lord says: "It is not a curse — it is a blessing for Me:
you have fulfilled My desire; do not fear."

**65.** Hearing this, the brahmana fell at the Lord's feet,
and Mahaprabhu, raising him, embraced him.

**66.** In the Lord's embrace the brahmana is beside himself with prema:
overflowing with prema for Krishna, he melted.

**67.** Bhagavan fulfilled the brahmana's desire —
He gave him prema, hard even for Brahma to obtain.

**68.** Such wondrous pastimes Gaurangasundara performs —
scoundrels with wicked hearts cannot understand them.

**69.** Thus Mahaprabhu rejoiced at heart —
and Lochana Dasa happily sings of Gora's qualities.

## Shachi's Grief over the Brahmana's Curse

*Raga — any (yatha-raga)*

**69+1.** Hearing from people's lips of the brahmana's curse on the Lord,
Shachi suddenly trembled to the depths of her soul[^33-4].

**69+2.** Her heart pounds and burns — she does not know what has happened;
she cannot hold back her tears — both eyes are full.

**69+3.** In distress Shachi asks everyone —
and on everyone's lips is the brahmana's curse on the Lord.

**69+4.** Hearing this, she fell there senseless;
coming to herself, Shachi weeps aloud.

**69+5.** Weeping, she returned home;
a little later Vishvambhara came home too.

**69+6.** Seeing Gaura's face, the mother's grief welled up,
and, weeping, Shachi began to ask:

**69+7.** "Listen, my son Nimai, what is this I hear?
They say a brahmana has pronounced a curse on you?

**69+8.** What offence did you commit against him?
What sort of brahmana is he — how hard his heart!

**69+9.** Looking at your face, he felt no pity!
Who will be guilty of my death?

**69+10.** All this house and household of mine is only for you;
the heart of unfortunate Shachi is breaking.

**69+11.** You are everyone's darling, the apple of my eye:
may I not lose you by some evil turn of fate!

**69+12.** Looking at your face, I bathe as if in nectar —
and hearing cruel words, my breast is bursting.

**69+13.** I know not what fate awaits unfortunate Shachi:
if harm comes to you, I shall die."

**69+14.** Hearing these words, beautiful Gauranga
gives His mother this answer in consolation:

**69+15.** "Listen, mother, to My word:
why do you weep for nothing?

**69+16.** I have committed no offence against the brahmana:
the brahmana cursed Me without cause.

**69+17.** And how can a curse take effect without an offence?
Know for certain, mother: this is a true word"[^33-5].

**69+18.** Saying this, the Lord went to the bank of the Jahnavi
and, bathing in the heavenly river, came back home.

**69+19.** Returning home, Mahaprabhu with great reverence
and joy in His heart performs the worship of Krishna.

**69+20.** Having worshipped, He recited hymns for a while
and, absorbed in prema, watered the tulasi.

**69+21.** Having bowed down, the Lord took refreshment,
and Shachi gazes tenderly at her son's face.

**69+22.** The radiance of Gora's face outshines millions of moons;
of Gauranga's deeds this Lochana Dasa tells.

## Mahaprabhu Distributes Prema in His Bhavas

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* Sriman Mahaprabhu [one day] danced together [with the devotees] in the bhava of Varaha. Then, [saying] that Shiva, Shuka, Narada, Brahma and other sages worship God by song, that the best [dharma] of the Kali age is the sankirtana of the name, and that [Gaurahari] has descended to distribute this dharma at the door of every living being, Sriman Mahaprabhu ordered the devotees headed by Advaita Acharya [to preach it] and, singing with His own the stories of the gopis, in the bhava of a gopi He went with sankirtana to the house of Chandrashekhara Acharya; [Srivasa] Pandita was filled with the bhava of Narada. In His bhava Sriman Mahaprabhu glorified before all the greatness of Gadadhara Pandita and revealed that he is Radhika, [worshipped by] Brahma, Shiva, Narada and others. Then Thakura Haridasa [came], and all the Vaishnavas joined them in the sankirtana of the name and prema. [Dancing] in the bhava of a gopi, [the Lord] suddenly became filled with the bhava of His majesty; then the devotees saw Him in the form of Lakshmi and offered praise to Lakshmi. [In the bhava of Lakshmi the Lord] distributed the prema of service; at that time someone came and loudly called Sriman Mahaprabhu "Prabhu!" — and [the Lord, giving up] that bhava, in the bhava of Ishvara distributed prema to all.[^33-6]

*Raga vibhasa; disha: "Glory, glory to Gaurachand, risen in Nadia in the Kali age!" (murchha); refrain: "Hear the story of my Lord, whose qualities have lit up the three worlds! Hear the story of Gaurangachand!"*

**1.** I shall tell another story — listen, it is most wondrous:
in the town of Nadia every day there is a new marvel.

**2.** The Lord sits in His house, joyful at heart,
and all His own sit around Him.

**3.** Suddenly a cry rose to the sky:
in a voice like a thundercloud He calls: "Give Me honey!"

**4.** At that very moment the Lord assumes the form of Halayudha —
clad in blue, like a white mountain,[^33-7]

**5.** with beautiful feet and lotus eyes;
seeing this marvel, all rejoiced at heart.

**6.** The Giver of prema to all enjoys prema:
in His bhava the Great Soul dances.

**7.** Singing the name of Hari with all His own,
thus He went to Advaita and Murari.[^33-8]

**8.** Going there, the Lord speaks in a faltering voice,
crying "Give, give Me honey!" — and laughs aloud.

**9.** The colour of His body is like the rising sun;
"Give, give Me honey!" He says, holding out His hand.

**10.** Taking in His hand a vessel full of water,
He drinks [it as] honey and belches with relish.[^33-9]

**11.** Reeling, He dances, intoxicated with prema,
and belches with relish: "heu, heu!"

**12.** Now He falls, now rises, now weeps, now laughs,
now, smacking His lips, laughs aloud.

**13.** Seeing this, all the people offer praise,
and some, crying "Haladhara!", clasp His feet.

**14.** Then Mahaprabhu, playing Balarama,
speaks incomparable, nectarean words:

**15.** "I am not Sri Krishna, to be pleased with words —
bring Me wonderful, sweet honey!"[^33-10]

**16.** A brahmana was standing there;
"Here is a wrestler!" [the Lord] said, and pushed him with a finger.

**17.** At the push of a finger the brahmana flew far off;
the brahmana was ashamed: the Thakura had thrown him down.

**18.** This bhava began in the morning [and lasted] until evening:
the Great Soul sports, playing Balarama.

**19.** The lotus feet of Narahari are the ornament of my head;
this Lochana Dasa sings of Gora's blessed qualities.

**20.** Hear one more wonder — on the following day
the Thakura dances, imitating Baladeva.

**21.** Suddenly, in grief, He fainted;
remembering Balarama, He sheds tears.

**22.** Mahaprabhu rolls on the ground with His hair loose;
they sprinkle water on His face — all are in distress.

**23.** In a moment, seeing Gadadhara, He came to Himself
and, understanding [his] silent sign, spoke anguished words:

**24.** "You are My friend — I know you are as dear to Me as life;
I am conquered by your love, listen, jewel of brahmanas.

**25.** I am your Lord, you are My life;
[people] call Me 'Gadai's Gauranga' — mark this.

**26.** None of My bhavas is hidden from you:
your body is My inner shakti.[^33-11]

**27.** Day and night you do not leave Me for a moment:
who but you truly knows My ways?

**28.** All My dear friends, the Vaishnavas —
bring them; I wish to see them now."

**29.** Receiving the command, Gadadhara Pandita
brought them all — Acharyaratna and the others.[^33-12]

**30.** Coming, all those great souls saw [Him] —
and all were overwhelmed, their eyes filled with tears.

**31.** Acharyaratna spoke gentle words:
"Tell us Yourself, dear child, what is the cause?"

**32.** Hearing his words, Vishvambhara speaks,
but cannot speak — His voice chokes in His throat.

**33.** In great agitation He speaks in broken words:
"I saw Halayudha, like a white mountain — beside Me.

**34.** The radiance of His body, golden, is like the sun,
His ornaments flash brilliantly."

**35.** As He spoke, the Lord again
saw Baladeva, like a white mountain.

**36.** Then Mahaprabhu Vishvambhara-raya
again, in that bhava, dances and sings.

**37.** All the Vaishnavas are overwhelmed with bliss:
in prema for Balarama all of them reel.

**38.** Bliss filled all the quarters;
for two days the Lord's bhava did not break.

**39.** Then on the following day, at the time of dancing,
all the great devotees surrounded Him on every side.

**40.** Under the weight of His feet the earth shakes;
He rolls His reddened eyes and speaks in broken words.

**41.** His gait is slow like that of an intoxicated elephant:
He cannot walk, absorbed in prema.

**42.** As the Lord is given over to His bhava, so are His companions:
beside Himself, He dances, delighting in the play of Balarama.

**43.** With dancing and singing evening came,
and suddenly from His mouth there wafted the fragrance of varuni.

**44.** Everything was filled with the divine fragrance of varuni;
the people look about in amazement.

**45.** All ten directions are fragrant with the scent of varuni;
the devotees grew intoxicated in the madness of prema.[^33-13]

**46.** At that time Sri Rama Pandita, the best of brahmanas,
saw — hear what was revealed to him:

**47.** suddenly there appeared there
divine, divine jewels among men in divine dress:

**48.** each had a lotus in one ear, lotus eyes,
an earring in the other ear, blue garments;

**49.** on their heads yellow turbans, loosely tied —
I cannot describe the beauty and elegance of their forms and dress.

**50.** There was a brahmana there named Vanamali —
I shall tell of him; listen, all brothers:

**51.** he saw a body made of gold,
adorned with gems like the peak of Sumeru.[^33-14]

**52.** Seeing it, he rejoiced at heart, his body thrilled,
and all the people, seeing it, were amazed.

**53.** In the form of Halayudha the Lord of the three worlds dances,
and all the devotees together dance with Him.

**54.** The gods in the sky, rejoicing at heart,
went back content to their own abodes.

**55.** Thus, having spent the whole day and night,
the Lord, smiling, goes to bathe in the heavenly river.

**56.** All the Vaishnavas together
dip and bathe in the waters of the heavenly river.

**57.** The Lord laughs and jests with His own
and plays merrily, delighting in their company.

**58.** Finishing His bath, the Lord quickly came out [of the water];
all bowed to the Lord and went to their homes.

**59.** The Lord, going home, remains in great happiness;
in the morning all came before the Lord.

**60.** And Mahaprabhu said: "Hear one word" —
but His voice choked, and only half was distinct:

**61.** "Lord Varaha embraced Me,
Halayudha entered My heart.

**62.** He who holds the flute to His lips has become the collyrium of My eyes."[^33-15]
Thus He told a nectarean story — hear, His own!

**63.** Seeing Srivasa, Mahaprabhu said:
"Give Me My flute!" — and holds out His divine hand.

**64.** Then Srinivasa Pandita Thakura,
a clever devotee, answered Him:

**65.** "Listen, listen, Mahaprabhu: here, in Your house,
the daughter of Bhishmaka keeps Your flute for You.[^33-16]

**66.** At night the doors of the house were locked —
You will get the flute at once, I tell You."

**67.** Thus every moment there is joy and merriment:
most wondrous are these pastimes in Nadia.

**68.** Whoever knows the rasa of Krishna knows their essence:
the story of the pastimes in Nadia is a great treasure.

**69.** And those who do not know — I implore them:
do not neglect it, turn your minds to Gora's qualities.

**70.** Consider with your heart, brothers, what lies in this:
you will get into your hands Krishna, the Lord of the three worlds.

**71.** Without serving [Him] there is no, no, no deliverance —
so says Lochana Dasa again and again.

[^33-1]: Vv. 45–69 — cf. Murari 2.13.18–23; Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 17. The kirtana in Srivasa's house was held behind closed doors, and outsiders were not admitted.
[^33-2]: Tearing the sacred thread is the sign of a curse pronounced by an angry brahmana.
[^33-3]: "Outside samsara" means one who has renounced the world, a sannyasi; the Lord Himself wished to take sannyasa, so the curse is a blessing for Him (cf. Murari 2.13.21–22). The brahmana, coming to his senses, himself foretells the Lord's sannyasa and its fruit: the disputants and logicians who did not honour Him as a householder will bow before the sannyasi and receive prema (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 17).
[^33-4]: This section is not in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition (1922); it is translated from Haridasa Shastri's edition (1983), where it stands without a heading of its own (only with the indication "যথা রাগ" and a fresh numbering, vv. 1–22) right after the story of the brahmana's curse (vv. 45–69); the heading is ours. The 1903 edition lacks it. Shachi, full of maternal love (vatsalya), fears for her son as any mother would; the Lord consoles her.
[^33-5]: Consoling His mother, the Lord says that a curse without an offence will not take effect; but He Himself, as said above (vv. 56, 64), accepted this curse as a blessing, since it coincided with His wish to take sannyasa (see note 3). Then (vv. 69+19 — 69+20) the Lord, playing the role of a devotee, Himself worships Krishna and the tulasi, teaching this to others.
[^33-6]: This summary concerns pastimes told in the next portion (the bhava of Varaha, the sankirtana, the play in Chandrashekhara's house and so on); the pastimes in the bhava of Balarama with which the section begins (vv. 1–58) are already described in the summary of the previous section (en/32). The OCR of the summary is badly garbled; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface ("the Lord's bhava of Varaha, the order to Advaita Acharya and other devotees about sankirtana and preaching, the singing of the gopis' qualities in the bhava of a gopi, the visit to Chandrashekhara's house, Srivasa's bhava of Narada, the glorification of Gadadhara and the revelation that he is Radhika, the arrival of Thakura Haridasa, the bliss of sankirtana, the Lord's bhava of majesty, the distribution of the prema of service in the form of Lakshmi, and finally the bhava of Ishvara").
[^33-7]: Halayudha ("armed with the plough"), Haladhara and Baladeva are Balarama. The form and bhava of Balarama that Gaura reveals are His own: Balarama is the first manifestation of Krishna Himself. Cf. Murari 2.14.
[^33-8]: So in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition ("অদ্বৈত-মুরারির স্থানে"; the OCR is not entirely clear); the 1983 edition has "to Advaita Acharya", Murari (2.14.4) the house of Murari; Bhaktisiddhanta's summary, the house of Advaita Acharya.
[^33-9]: Balarama loves the intoxicating drink varuni; the Lord in His bhava drinks water as honey (cf. Murari 2.14.5). This is not worldly intoxication but rapture in prema (cf. en/28, note 12).
[^33-10]: Cf. Murari 2.14.7: "I am not that Krishna whom it is enough to please with words."
[^33-11]: Gadadhara Pandita is, in the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding, Sri Radha (en/25, note 7), the inner (antaranga) shakti of the Lord; hence all His bhavas are known to him. "Gadai's Gauranga" means "Gadadhara's Gauranga".
[^33-12]: Acharyaratna is Chandrashekhara Acharya. Vv. 20–38 — cf. Murari 2.14.10–15.
[^33-13]: Vv. 43–54 — cf. Murari 2.14.17–22. Balarama's divine varuni is no worldly wine; its fragrance is the sign of the presence of Balarama and His companions, whom Sri Rama Pandita, Srivasa's brother, sees.
[^33-14]: In Murari (2.14.20) Vanamali sees on the ground Balarama's golden club, Saunanda. Whether this is the same Vanamali as in en/31 (v. 6 of the section) is not stated.
[^33-15]: That is, the Lord ceaselessly beholds Krishna with the flute. Cf. Murari 2.15.1: the Lord tells of the vision of Varaha and Halayudha and asks for His flute.
[^33-16]: The daughter of Bhishmaka is Rukmini; thus Srivasa jokingly refers to the Lord's wife, Vishnupriya. (According to the Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika, Rukmini appeared in Gaura-lila as Lakshmipriya, and Vishnupriya is Bhu-shakti.)

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 11 (portion 34)

*The section "Mahaprabhu Distributes Prema in His Bhavas" (end): the Lord tells the devotees of the greatness of the sankirtana-yajna; the play in Chandrashekhara's house — the Lord in a gopi's dress, Srivasa in the bhava of Narada glorifies Gadadhara as Radha; the arrival of Haridasa and Advaita; the Lord in the bhava of Lakshmi and of the primal Shakti, Haridasa on Her lap; the bhava of Ishvara; Lochana Dasa's reflection on the hidden avatara*

**72.** The next day the Lord, seated on a divine seat,
began to speak to all the devotees:

**73.** "Such is the greatness of this sankirtana-yajna of Mine:[^34-1]
all the scriptures speak of its greatness and glory.

**74.** This dharma of sankirtana is the essence of all dharmas;
know that in the Kali age this above all is the work to do.

**75.** It is revealed from the fifth Veda[^34-2] —
therefore Shiva sings [it] ceaselessly with his five mouths.

**76.** Narada wanders dancing, singing to his vina;
the devotees — Shuka, Sanaka and others — wander singing.

**77.** In Vrindavana Radha and Krishna, taking up this Veda,
dance with the gopis, absorbed in prema.

**78.** Know that the fifth [Veda] abides eternally in Vrindavana —
therefore Shiva sings in great prema.

**79.** And yet, for all his singing, Shiva did not reach its end —
such a Veda has appeared in the Kali age.

**80.** Whoever performs it in song is awakened:
the utterance of the Veda in the form of song is a great mercy.

**81.** The hollows of everyone's ears are the expanse of the sacrificial pit,
the tongue is the ladle, the sweet rasa of the sound is the ghee.[^34-3]

**82.** Entering within, it kindles the fire of bhava;
its flames are thrill and tears, the trembling of the body.

**83.** Freed from all sins, all the people dance;
the liberations — salokya and the rest — follow them about,

**84.** but they never glance at them even from the corner of their eyes:
they wander dancing, tasting the rasa of Krishna.[^34-4]

**85.** The Vaishnava acharyas stand around this yajna;
know that the yajna of kirtana is the noblest of all yajnas.

**86.** From it this great treasure, prema, was born;
the master of this [yajna] is Nityananda with His circle,

**87.** and the mistress of this prema is Gadadhara Pandita;[^34-5]
know this truth, all you jewels among devotees.

**88.** Advaita Acharya Gosani, having brought Me down,
firmly establishes the sankirtana-yajna.[^34-6]

**89.** Srinivasa, Narahari and the other devotees —
with all of you I establish My yajna.

**90.** Carry this yajna in the Kali age from house to house —
let all the people, the fallen and the sinful, be delivered."

**91.** Hearing this, the devotees, weeping and weeping,
fall at the Lord's feet, swaying.

**92.** Gaura Bhagavan embraced them all;
hearing this joyful story, this Lochana sings.

*Raga varadi; the tune "dhulakhela"*

**93.** Another wondrous story — hear the song of Gora's qualities,
words unknown to the world and to the Vedas.
In the sway of His bhava, to proclaim bhakti-yoga,
He acts — mercy embodied, the jewel of all qualities.

**94.** Hear the story attentively, leaving other talk:
it is time to tell the rest as well.
Together with His own, Srila Vishvambhara Hari
went to the house of Sri Chandrashekhara.[^34-7]

**95.** In the course of talk the conversation turned to the qualities of the gopis —
and, speaking of them, His voice faltered;
His face grew red, tears streamed from His eyes —
immersed in rasa, He revealed rasa.

**96.** The Lord whose feet Kamala serves in rapture —
such a Lord, for the gopis' sake,
is overwhelmed at the mere mention [of them]: such was the devotion they showed!
At mere words He takes on their bhava.

**97.** Then Vishvambhara Hari, putting on a gopi's dress,
in the house of Sri Chandrashekhara Acharya
dances, lost in bliss; and at that very hour
the bhava of Narada came upon Srivasa.[^34-8]

**98.** He bows to the Lord and humbly says:
"Know me as Your servant."
Having spoken these words, that great sage
then addresses Gadadhara Pandita:

**99.** "Listen, gopi, to what I shall say:
do you know anything of your past?
I shall tell a wonder: you are rare in the world —
hear attentively about yourself.

**100.** Hear about all of you — I shall sing your qualities:
you were born, one and all, in Gokula;
leaving your fidelity to your husbands, you served [Krishna] unceasingly
and attained your heart's desire in Vrindavana.[^34-9]

**101.** You are the first of prakritis, you are Radha, Krishna's shakti:[^34-10]
what do I know, that I could tell of it?

**102.** Crown of all women, the favoured one in Krishna's love —
how could I tell your truth?

**103.** You showed such devotion that none can equal [you];
it is the supreme secret of the three worlds.
Brahma, the god Maheshvara, Lakshmi and even Ananta
have less grace than you.

**104.** Prahlada, Narada and others, Sanatana, Shuka and the rest
know not a drop of your devotion.
The Husband of the Lakshmi of the three worlds longs for your love —
and puts this lovely dress on His own body.[^34-11]

**105.** He whose maidservant is Lakshmi, hoping for your prema,
holds in His heart a longing [for you];
the Lord of all the worlds you have enchanted with that love —
blessed, blessed is your tenderness!

**106.** You have known the truth — the greatness of the Lord's inmost heart —
and have bound [Him] fast with love.
Uddhava, Akrura and the rest — all serve your feet:
do not forsake in your heart those who follow you."

**107.** Such words spoke Srinivasa, the jewel of brahmanas;
hearing them, all rejoiced.
All the Vaishnavas together embrace one another,
gazing at Vishvambhara's feet.

**108.** They dance, lost in bliss, overflowing with prema —
and at that hour Haridasa came.
Staff in hand, he stood in front and proclaims,
singing [the Lord's] qualities in supreme delight:[^34-12]

**109.** "Brothers, perform the sankirtana of Hari's qualities without cease!" —
so saying, he laughs aloud.
Lost in singing Hari's qualities, he sheds streams from his eyes
and in joy turns round to every side.

**110.** Hearing Haridasa's words, all the jewel-like Vaishnavas —
as if their whole bodies were sprinkled with nectar:
in joy they dance and sing, in their midst dances Gora-raya,
and, weeping, they clasp His reddish feet.

**111.** Then came the abode of all virtues, named Advaita Acharya,
the king of all the Vaishnavas;
His beauty lit up the earth; He stands in front and gazes —
born of the Lord's portion, of great might.[^34-13]

**112.** "Hari, Hari!" He calls, and the people start;
in joy He dances, filled with prema;
His whole body thrills from feet to head,
tears of prema stream from His eyes.

**113.** Again and again He gazes at Vishvambhara's holy feet,
roars and slaps His arms like a wrestler;
all the Vaishnavas together, opening baskets of the wares of prema,
spread out a wondrous market.

**114.** All the Vaishnavas, rejoicing at heart,
plunged into the ocean of prema;
with all the Vaishnavas Sri Gaurahari Himself
reveals happiness to the world.

**115.** And now I shall tell — listen, all, attentively:
the Lord, in the sway of a gopi's bhava,
puts a bodice on His breast, conch-shell bangles on His wrists;
both His eyes are drowning in rasa.

**116.** He wears silk, anklets on His feet,
His waist so slender a fist could clasp it;
His beauty charms the three worlds — to what can it be compared? —
the Thakura Himself in a gopi's dress.[^34-14]

**117.** From the unearthly radiance of His body a Malaya breeze blows,
on Him is a garland of fresh malati;
like the waters of the heavenly river on the peak of Sumeru,
two streams [of tears] flow over Gora's body.

**118.** Amid all the Vaishnavas dances the great king of dancers,
taking on bhavas in the rapture of rasa.
And as He danced thus, He thought of Lakshmi —
and in that bhava went into the Deity's chamber.

**119.** Entering the chamber and seeing the divine four-armed Deity,
He stood beside It;
He looks with half-closed eyes, walks with half-steps,
and has covered His eyes with the edge of His cloth.[^34-15]

**120.** Then all His own, falling at His holy feet,
offer praise with humble words;
someone recites the Sri-stava and, lost in bliss,
asks a boon: "Give prema-bhakti!"

**121.** All offer praise; hearing it, at that very moment
He thought of the primal Shakti
and took on that very bhava; all are amazed
and recite many hymns composed by the gods.[^34-16]

**122.** When all had offered praise with the great hymns of the gods,
the primal Shakti, pleased, speaks;
seated on the Deity's seat, She says with a soft smile:
"I have come to see [your] prema-bhakti.

**123.** I have come wishing to see your dancing and singing —
I have told you My desire."
Hearing this, all those people say again:
"Reveal [in us] devotion to You."

**124.** When they asked this boon, the primal Shakti said:
"Listen, listen, listen, all!
I am Chandi, the fierce: you too shall have the rod!" —
such was the boon She gave to all.[^34-17]

**125.** Hearing this, all bow down,
falling to the ground like a stick.
Then that Mistress, taking Haridasa by the hand,
smiling, seated him on Her lap.

**126.** Sitting on Her lap, Haridasa rocks to and fro
like a child of five.
Seeing this marvel, all rejoiced at heart;
even the birds and beasts were glad.

**127.** At that moment someone spoke these words:
"Cast a merciful glance on Murari:
he is Your own servant." Hearing this, [She] smiled
softly, sweeter than nectar.

**128.** Her eyes are full of tears of compassion, brimming with prema,
the tender moon of Her face grew red.
At that hour Shachi-devi, herself serving [His] holy feet,
was overcome by the bliss of prema.

**129.** Then that Katyayani, drawing all near,
regards them as Her own sons;
She shows the people a mother's tenderness, and all, looking at Her,
fill their eyes with tears of prema.[^34-18]

**130.** At that hour, at that very moment, a brahmana came
and loudly called: "Prabhu!"
Seeing the suffering of the sufferer, His eyes filled with tears,
and He entered the rapture [of the bhava] of Ishvara.

**131.** Becoming Ishvara Himself, revealing His prema,
He shows His lordship by His own qualities.
All, surrounding Him, prostrated themselves,
seeing again [in Him] the bhava of Ishvara.

**132.** Thus, spending the whole night in the rapture of rasa,
at dawn He went home.
All who went with Him saw Gora-raya
as if holding a fierce rod.[^34-19]

**133.** Thus Gaurahari, the one Maker of all the worlds,
revealed His mercy;
having come for mercy's sake and showing humility,
He Himself cares for the earth.

**134.** If anyone, hearing so wondrous a story, is not freed
in his heart from the pain of samsara —
he will never be freed from it; and whoever doubts this —
shame on him: there is no worse wretch.

**135.** Reason, experience and scripture — all three say just this,
though one does not see it openly proclaimed;
whoever does not ponder this — that what never was has come to be —
how will such a one be delivered?[^34-20]

**136.** Such an avatara as Gora, such a revelation of mercy,
has never been and never will be.
Let people say what they will — I tell them what I have realised:
let them reflect on it within themselves.

**137.** One care I have, a pain deep in my heart:
lest such an avatara pass [people] by.
For this my heart weeps — to whom shall I tell it?
This Lochana Dasa sings [Gora's] qualities.

[^34-1]: Cf. Bhagavata 11.5.32: in the age of Kali the intelligent worship the Lord by a yajna consisting chiefly of sankirtana. Bhaktisiddhanta's summary of these pastimes is at the head of the section (en/33).
[^34-2]: The "fifth Veda" usually means the itihasas and puranas (Bhagavata 1.4.20); Lochana Dasa gives this name to the sung word, the Veda "in the form of song" (v. 80) revealed in the Kali age. "With five mouths" is a play on words: Shiva has five faces.
[^34-3]: Kirtana is described as a yajna: the sacrificial pit (kunda), the ladle (sruva) with which ghee is poured into the fire, and the ghee itself.
[^34-4]: The pure devotees do not accept the five kinds of liberation (salokya, sarupya and so on) if they contain no service to the Lord (Bhagavata 3.29.13).
[^34-5]: The image is that of a Vedic yajna, which the master (yajamana) performs together with his wife. Nityananda is the giver of prema; Gadadhara Pandita is, in the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding, Sri Radha (en/25, note 7), whose prema this is.
[^34-6]: Advaita Acharya (Maha-Vishnu, en/27, note 8) brought the Lord down into this world by His calls.
[^34-7]: Chandrashekhara Acharya (Acharyaratna) is the husband of Shachi's sister, the Lord's aunt. The play in his house: Murari 2.15.7–19, 2.16; Chaitanya-bhagavata, Madhya 18 (where the Lord appears in the bhava of Rukmini and of the primal Shakti, Gadadhara as Her companion, Haridasa as a watchman with a staff, Srivasa as Narada). Bhaktisiddhanta's summary is at the head of the section (en/33).
[^34-8]: According to the Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika, Srivasa is Narada. The gopi's dress and the bhavas of a gopi, of Lakshmi and of the primal Shakti which the Lord shows in this play are His own lila: the shakti is non-different from the Possessor of shakti, and He reveals the bhavas of His shaktis. This is no worldly dressing-up or play-acting.
[^34-9]: The gopis' love for Krishna is pure prema, above all worldly duties; their "husbands" are a mere appearance created by yogamaya (Bhagavata 10.33.37). There is nothing worldly here.
[^34-10]: Prakriti here means Krishna's shakti, His internal energy, not material nature (the pradhana of Sankhya). Gadadhara Pandita is Sri Radha (en/25, note 7). In A this stanza is printed under two numbers, 101 and 102 (one per half-stanza); in the 1983 edition it is a single stanza (9). We follow A's numbering; next, A misprints "100" for 103.
[^34-11]: That is, the Lord, longing for Radha's love, has Himself put on a gopi's dress — a hint at the secret of Gaura's appearance, in which He took Radha's bhava and complexion (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 1.5–6).
[^34-12]: Cf. Murari 2.16.1. In the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya 18) Haridasa in this play is a watchman-herald with a staff.
[^34-13]: Advaita Acharya is Maha-Vishnu (en/27, note 8); the word "portion" does not diminish Him. Cf. Murari 2.16.3–4.
[^34-14]: Cf. Murari 2.16.7–8.
[^34-15]: Cf. Murari 2.16.11–13. The half-closed eyes, half-steps and veil are the feminine modesty of Lakshmi, whose bhava the Lord reveals.
[^34-16]: The primal Shakti (Adya-shakti) — in Lochana Dasa she is also Chandi and Katyayani (vv. 124, 129). In the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding the Lord's primal shakti is His internal energy (yogamaya); Durga, who rules the material world, is her shadow and the Lord's servant (Brahma-samhita 5.44). The Lord reveals the bhava of His own shakti, non-different from Him (cf. Murari 2.16.15 and note 8 above); this is neither possession nor the worship of another deity.
[^34-17]: The rod (danda) is a sign of power and fierce might; the 1983 edition has "you will all become fierce". In Murari (2.16.17–18) the Goddess jokes that people will take Her devotees for worshippers of Chandi. Cf. also v. 132.
[^34-18]: According to Murari (2.16.20–21) and the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya 18), the Lord in this bhava gave the devotees milk from His breast, like a mother.
[^34-19]: Cf. Murari 2.16.23: the Lord, as if holding a staff in His hand, seemed to the people like the flame of the blazing sun.
[^34-20]: Gaura is the hidden (channa) avatara: the scriptures speak of Him covertly (Bhagavata 7.9.38; 11.5.32). The line "না ছিল সে হৈল সিয়া" is unclear in all witnesses; we translate by sense.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 12 (portion 35)

*The section "Before Sannyasa": seven days of radiance in Chandrashekhara's house; Srivasa asks about the dharmas of the yugas, and the Lord proclaims sankirtana; the Lord's longing for Vrindavana and Murari's persuasion; the dream of the sannyasa mantra; the arrival of Keshava Bharati; Mukunda reveals the Lord's resolve to the devotees; the devotees' lament and the Lord's answer: He is burning in separation from Krishna*

## Before Sannyasa
