> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* In answer to Srivasa Pandita's question Sriman Mahaprabhu told of the dharmas of the four yugas [and revealed that] in the Kali age [the best dharma] is the sankirtana of the name; [then,] in the bhava of Radha, He became agitated, [crying,] "Where is Vrindavana? Where is Lalita? Where is Govardhana?" Then, at Murari's words, Sriman Mahaprabhu grew calm and [again] gave Himself fully to [kirtana] with the Vaishnavas, and one day He told [His mother] that He had received the sannyasa mantra in a dream. Some days later Keshava Bharati came to Sriman Mahaprabhu's house, and the Lord received him fittingly. [At the sight of the sannyasi] Sriman Mahaprabhu's separation from Krishna became unbearable. Learning that Mahaprabhu would take sannyasa, the devotees, in fear of the coming separation from Him, began to lament bitterly, and the Lord, telling them [of the pain of separation from Krishna and of the nature of samsara], consoled them.[^35-1]

*Raga varadi; refrain: "My life is Gorachand!"*

**1.** I shall tell a wondrous story, unknown to the world —
never seen or heard of anywhere on earth.

**2.** Let no one doubt it for a moment in his heart:
the Lord revealed this for the good of all people.

**3.** Having danced and sung His fill in Chandrashekhara's house,
the Lord returned home in joy.

**4.** Sri Chandrashekhara Bhattacharya rejoices —
I shall tell of the marvel in his house.

**5.** The Lord had come there to dance, and from His radiance
it was as if moons had risen — hundreds of thousands of moons.[^35-2]

**6.** A wondrous, cool light, more delightful than nectar;
one cannot look at it — as if lightning flashed on all four sides.

**7.** It gladdens the heart — one longs to look,
but one cannot open one's eyes: the brilliance prevents it.

**8.** The people of the town of Nadia were astonished:
what a marvel they had seen in these days!

**9.** Coming there, everyone asks the Vaishnavas:
"Do you know what this means? Tell us, then!"

**10.** All the Vaishnavas answer: "What do we know?
Vishvambhara, the jewel of qualities, came here to dance —

**11.** this alone we know, and nothing more:
His deeds are unknown to the world and to the Vedas."

**12.** For seven days this sea of radiance did not break;
for its brilliance no one could tell day from night.

**13.** Ever new, ever more joyful deeds
the son of Shachi reveals — the dharma of mercy.

**14.** The next day Srinivasa, the best of brahmanas,
asks the Thakura about what was in his heart:

**15.** "In the Kali age the sankirtana of Hari's name and qualities —
why is it said to give the full fruit, and [the ways of] the other yugas less?"

**16.** Hearing this, the Thakura says: "Listen, Srinivasa:
you have asked a good question — I shall explain in detail.

**17.** In the Satya age, when dharma was complete, they attained by meditation alone;
in the Treta age the generous-minded practise the dharma of yajna;

**18.** in the Dvapara, it is said, the essence [of dharma] is the worship of Krishna;
but in the Kali age no one is capable of these works.

**19.** The Thakura Himself is Bhagavan in the form of the name:
in the Kali age the name of Hari is endowed with all powers.

**20.** In the three ages beginning with Satya, all the great souls
served Narayana by meditation, yajna and worship according to rule.

**21.** In the sinful Kali age people's conduct is wicked —
and for this very reason mercy has turned the other way.[^35-3]

**22.** The Thakura Himself is in His own form of sankirtana —
and in the Kali age all perfections are attained without effort.

**23.** What in Satya and the other ages is attained with great hardship,
by the Lord's mercy is attained with ease in the Kali age."[^35-4]

**24.** Placing the lotus feet of Narahari on my head,
Lochana Dasa tells of the sweetness of Gauranga.

**25.** Thus in joy and gladness the days go by —
and suddenly sorrow rises in the Lord's heart:

**26.** "I cannot, I cannot stay here —
I shall go to see the land of Vrindavana!

**27.** Where is My Kalindi, the Yamuna, Vrindavana?
Where are My Bahula, Bhandira, Govardhana?

**28.** Where have My Radha, Lalita and the others gone?
Where have My Nanda and Yashoda gone?

**29.** Where have My Shridama and Sudama stayed?" —
and calling "Dhavali! Shangali!" He runs in longing.[^35-5]

**30.** Now, holding a straw between His teeth, piteously
He sobs aloud, looking round on every side:

**31.** "How shall I leave this round of worldly existence?
Where shall I go to attain the feet of Nanda's son?"

**32.** Saying this, He tore the sacred thread from His neck —
so unbearable had the grief of separation from Krishna become.[^35-6]

**33.** Calling "Hari, Hari!" He heaves deep sighs;
tears pour down in streams, but He says nothing distinct.

**34.** His body thrills, His face has grown red;
seeing this, Murari speaks these words to Him:

**35.** "Listen, listen, Mahaprabhu, Gaura Bhagavan:
nothing is impossible for You — [but] I shall speak of the consequences.

**36.** You are free both to stay and to go —
and yet do not disregard my words.

**37.** If You now leave for distant lands,
all the Vaishnavas will grow self-willed at heart;

**38.** self-willed, they will do whatever they please,
and all will enter again the house of samsara.

**39.** All that You have done, Lord, will come to nothing —
this I tell You for certain."[^35-7]

**40.** Hearing this, the Lord fell silent:
He could not refute what Murari had said.

**41.** Thus several more days passed in merriment;
the people of Nadia could not look their fill of Him.

**42.** Gladdening His mother's heart and eyes,
Gaurahari passes His time in play with Vishnupriya.[^35-8]

**43.** With kinsmen and friends He lives in great happiness
and delights everyone in Navadvipa.

**44.** With the Vaishnavas — the pastimes of kirtana;
the ladies of the town, looking at Him, sigh with longing.[^35-9]

**45.** His form is the wonder of the three worlds, and in it is the charm of a nagara,
the very limit of playful grace, rasa and loveliness.

**46.** And on Him beautiful ornaments sparkle;
in the hair falling to His shoulders malati flowers gleam.

**47.** The sandal tilaka is drawn charmingly;
His cloth has a red border — the loveliest attire in the three worlds.

**48.** His own people and all the townsfolk —
each sees Him according to his own feeling.

**49.** And so, being with His own, the Lord
with a soft smile tells them all a dream:

**50.** "Listen, all: I saw a dream last night:
suddenly a jewel of a brahmana came to Me

**51.** and spoke into My ear a certain mantra of sannyasa;
even now it is vivid in My mind.

**52.** Since the mantra entered My ear,
My heart has not been My own.

**53.** How shall I leave My beloved Lord of life?
Leaving Him, what work would I do?

**54.** More beautiful than a sapphire, supremely beautiful,
He sits on My breast and smiles without cease."

**55.** Hearing this, Murari Gupta replied:
"Take that mantra as a compound with the genitive case."[^35-10]

**56.** Hearing this, the Lord said:
"Your words do not steady My mind.

**57.** The more I restrain Myself, the more the tears rise;
say nothing to Me — listen to Me:

**58.** such is the power of the words — what can I do?
I cannot transgress it, whatever you may say."

**59.** Hearing this, all were troubled at heart —
says Lochana Dasa, with pain in his heart.

**60.** Some days later Sri Keshava Bharati came,
the best of sannyasis, of utterly pure mind —

**61.** full of great splendour, the best of renunciants, a maha-bhagavata,
a mountain of merit gathered in former births.

**62.** Arriving unexpectedly, he saw Vishvambhara;
seeing Vishvambhara, the best of sannyasis rejoiced.

**63.** The Thakura rose and bowed at his feet;
at the sight of the sannyasi tears of prema streamed from His eyes.

**64.** That king of sannyasis gazes intently at the Lord:
the best of sannyasis, of great understanding, grasped how matters stood.

**65.** Keshava Bharati Gosani said:
"Are You not Shuka, or Prahlada? — so it seems to me."

**66.** Hearing this, Lord Vishvambhara
wept twice as hard — tears pour from His eyes.

**67.** Then the sannyasi, amazed, speaks again,
having reflected and become certain in his mind:

**68.** "You are the Lord, Bhagavan — I know it for certain;
You are the life of all people, there is no doubt."

**69.** Hearing this, the Lord wept:
"When shall I attain Krishna's feet?

**70.** Your love for Krishna is so great
that you see Krishna everywhere.[^35-11]

**71.** When shall I be able to see Krishna?
When shall I have such garb as yours?

**72.** In search of Krishna I shall go from land to land:
where shall I go to find Krishna, the Lord of My life?"

**73.** Having told the sannyasi [what lay in His heart], Vishvambhara,
the Lord, bowed to him to the ground and goes home.

**74.** Seeing Srivasa, the Lord said:
"Take the sannyasi to your house."

**75.** Hearing the Lord's words, Srivasa Thakura
took the sannyasi home and fed him abundantly.

**76.** Having taken his meal and spent that day there,
the best of renunciants went his way at dawn.[^35-12]

**77.** In the morning Srinivasa, coming to the Lord,
with folded hands told Him that the sannyasi had left.

**78.** Hearing this, the Lord, with longing in His heart,
thinking of the sannyasi, went home.

**79.** Coming home and reflecting within,
Gaurahari resolved: "I shall take sannyasa."

**80.** Mukunda understood it from hints and from [the Lord's] look;
to keep the Lord, he sought every possible means.

**81.** He came where all the devotees were
and, weeping, spoke, [falling] at the feet of all the devotees:

**82.** "Listen, listen, all, to what I say:
this Lord Vishvambhara will take sannyasa.

**83.** While He is here, look at Him to your eyes' content;
hear the words of His holy mouth to your ears' content.

**84.** The Lord will leave His home,
will leave His mother and all His servants."

**85.** Hearing this, all, with aching hearts,
took counsel together and pondered what to do.

**86.** "The independent Lord will stay in no one's power" —
saying this, the devotees fell into dread.

**87.** Falling on the ground, covered with dust, they weep:
"O Lord of our life, our Lord Vishvambhara!

**88.** Alas, alas, Mahaprabhu! Where are You going, forsaking [us]?
The serpent of Kali will seize and devour us.

**89.** In fear of Kali we took refuge in You, Lord;
in fear of You the serpent of Kali does not come near us now."

**90.** At that time Lord Vishvambhara came there;
seeing Srivasa Pandita, He said:

**91.** "Listen, listen, O dear brahmana Srinivasa:
I shall tell you one thing, if you will not be frightened.

**92.** To earn prema I shall go to distant lands,
and then I shall bring it to you — listen, best of brahmanas.

**93.** As a merchant boards a boat and sails to a far country,
enduring all manner of hardships to earn wealth,

**94.** and, bringing it home, maintains his kinsmen —
so I too shall bring and give you the wealth of prema."[^35-13]

**95.** Hearing this, Srivasa Pandita says:
"Lord, what is life if we cannot see You?

**96.** While a man lives, his kinsmen maintain him;
when he has left his body, they perform shraddha and tarpana for him.

**97.** It is to those who live that You will give the wealth of prema —
but without seeing You, all will die."

**98.** Mukunda says: "Lord, the body burns,
within, the life burns — and yet does not depart.

**99.** We are all low, wicked and ill-behaved;
and You are a deceiver, crafty-hearted: now we understand Your ways.[^35-14]

**100.** We simple folk, not understanding You,
took refuge in You, giving up the world;

**101.** giving up dharma and karma, we made Your feet our all —
and You, having made us outcasts, why do You forsake us?

**102.** Knowing from the scriptures that You are the Purifier of the fallen,
we took refuge in You, giving up all dharmas.

**103.** And now You are going away, forsaking us —
this is not right, Lord — I humbly say.

**104.** Not perceiving Your crafty mind, we took refuge in You —
but Your heart is harder than a thunderbolt, cruel.

**105.** Catching outside the fragrance of the lotus's nectar,
I thought it was the same within — so it was in my heart.

**106.** Now I know how hard Your heart is:
like a pot of poison with milk on top.

**107.** Like a wooden sweetball dusted with camphor:
one cannot swallow it, not seeing what it is.[^35-15]

**107+1.** As a wife of good family, blinded by desire,
enters into love with another man[^35-16]

**107+2.** and, abandoning dharma, duty and shame, acts accordingly —
and he, having disgraced her, abandons her in the end;

**107+3.** and that woman is left without support in both families —
so too will You set us all adrift on the shoreless [ocean].

**108.** If You go to distant lands, what use is life?
Why have You become cruel to all?"

**109.** "If I do not see Your face for even a moment, I die!" —
weeping and weeping, Murari says:

**110.** "Listen, listen, Vishvambhara, Gaura Bhagavan:
unworthy Murari speaks — pay heed!

**111.** You planted a wondrous tree, holding it with Your fingers,
You made it grow day and night, watering it and loosening the soil;

**112.** moment by moment You tended it, covered it with great care,
and laid all kinds of jewels about the tree's root —

**113.** and now, when the time of flowers and fruit has come, You cut the tree down:
we shall all die, our hearts will burst.

**114.** Ceaselessly, day and night, we know nothing else;
even in dreams we see Your moon-like face.

**115.** Worldly desires do not come near me:
[mine is] the breeze from Your feet, rare in the world.

**116.** You will go to distant lands, leaving everyone —
and the tiger of samsara will seize and devour us all.

**117.** You were merciful — why have You become pitiless?" —
saying this, they all together fell at [His] feet:

**118.** "O friend of the poor, Lord, master of the masterless!
O Deliverer of the fallen, Lord of the world!"

**119.** Some, with straw between their teeth, plead piteously,
some, raising their arms, call again and again.

**120.** The Lord says: "You are My very own servants;
I shall tell you My inmost conviction — listen."

**121.** As soon as He began to speak, His voice broke,
His reddish lotus eyes filled with tears.

**122.** In a piteous voice He speaks half-words;
unable to restrain Himself, for a moment He falls silent.

**123.** "You are distressed by fear of separation from Me —
but My body is in torment from separation from Krishna.

**124.** For your own happiness you cause Me pain:
what kind of love is this you have for Me, you people?

**125.** In separation from Krishna My heart burns;
My senses are scorched — a great fever has seized My body.

**126.** Even such a [loving] mother seems to Me like fire,
and your words seem mixed with poison.[^35-17]

**127.** Life without Krishna I do not count as life:
what use is such a life — like that of beasts and birds?

**128.** Like a corpse that has all its limbs;
so creepers, leaves and trees live by their breath.

**129.** Dharma and karma without Krishna are like a brahmana without the Vedas,
like a faithful wife without her husband, a fish without water.

**130.** There is no point in setting up a household without means;
it is like an unlearned man sitting in an assembly of the learned.

**131.** In separation from Krishna My life throbs —
whatever else you may say does not enter My ears.

**132.** Putting on a yogi's garb, I shall go from land to land —
to wherever I may find news of the Lord of My life."

**133.** Saying this, the Lord weeps, fallen on the ground,
and tore the sacred thread from His body.

**134.** "Krishna, Krishna!" He calls with an anguished cry,
and in a piteous voice weeps: "Lord of My life!"

[^35-1]: The OCR of the summary is badly garbled; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface ("the Lord, telling Srivasa of the dharmas of the four yugas, reveals the superiority of sankirtana; in the bhava of Radha, agitation [crying], 'Where is Vrindavana, where is Lalita?'; calmed by Murari's words — kirtana and pastimes; the account to mother Shachi of receiving the sannyasa mantra in a dream; the arrival of Keshava Bharati; the Lord's separation from Krishna; the devotees' grieving words in anxiety about the Lord's sannyasa; the Lord consoles the devotees"). In the poem the Lord tells the dream to all His own (vv. 49–50). In the 1983 edition vv. 1–24 belong to the previous chapter, and the new chapter ("सन्न्यास प्रसङ्ग") begins with v. 25.
[^35-2]: Vv. 1–13 — cf. Murari 2.17.1–4: for seven days the Lord's radiance did not fade in Chandrashekhara's house.
[^35-3]: The more wicked the age, the greater the Lord's mercy: He Himself comes as the holy name and sankirtana.
[^35-4]: Vv. 14–23 — cf. Murari 2.17.5–10; Bhagavata 12.3.52: what was attained by meditation in the Satya age, by yajnas in the Treta and by worship in the Dvapara is attained in the Kali age by the kirtana of Hari.
[^35-5]: Shridama and Sudama are Krishna's cowherd friends; Dhavali and Shangali are His cows. The Lord is in the bhava of separation from Vraja; Bhaktisiddhanta's summary says "in the bhava of Radha".
[^35-6]: Cf. Murari 2.17.12–13. The Lord tears off His thread once more in v. 133.
[^35-7]: Vv. 34–40 — cf. Murari 2.17.14–19: Murari reminds Him that people follow the Lord's example.
[^35-8]: Vishnupriya is the Lord's eternal consort, His shakti; her pastimes with the Lord are not worldly.
[^35-9]: On nagari-bhava see en/09, note 9.
[^35-10]: Cf. Murari 2.18.1–5. The sannyasa mantra in its wording speaks of identity with Brahman ("I am He"); Murari proposes to understand it through the genitive case (shashthi-samasa): not "I am He" but "I am His", that is, the eternal servant of the Lord. The Lord answers that the very power of the words will not let Him rest (v. 58).
[^35-11]: The Lord, playing the part of a devotee, deflects Keshava Bharati's praise and ascribes his vision to his own love for Krishna. Vv. 60–72 — cf. Murari 2.18.7–15.
[^35-12]: Keshava Bharati is the future sannyasa-guru of the Lord at Kantakanagara (Katwa). Vv. 73–79 — cf. Murari 2.18.15–16.
[^35-13]: Cf. Murari 2.18.19–22.
[^35-14]: The words "deceiver", "crafty", "cruel" (vv. 99–108) are reproaches of love (pranaya), like the gopis' reproaches to Krishna, not abuse: Mukunda speaks in the grief of separation.
[^35-15]: Both proverbs are about one who is sweet outside but different within. In A v. 107 is misprinted as "১০৮".
[^35-16]: Vv. 107+1 — 107+3 are found only in the 1983 edition (absent from Bhaktisiddhanta's edition); the 1903 edition has them in the main text. This worldly comparison is the language of loving reproach: the devotees, who for the Lord's sake gave up everything, fear being left without support (cf. the gopis' reproaches to Krishna before His departure for Mathura); the Lord Himself is not being spoken of as a worldly seducer.
[^35-17]: The extreme of separation from Krishna (vipralambha), when everything, even the dearest, burns like fire. Cf. Murari 2.17.12–13.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 13 (portion 36)

*The section "Before Sannyasa" (end): the Lord speaks of the cruelty of samsara and of how only service to Krishna gives meaning to human birth, and consoles the devotees. The section "Mother Shachi's Grief": Shachi's lament and her pleading; the Lord begins the story of Dhruva — Dhruva's hurt, his mother's counsel, the meeting with Narada, the journey to Madhuvana*

*Raga vibhasa, the taraja tune; disha: "na hare are haya"*

**135.** "Listen, all: cruel samsara
has put Me in peril;[^36-1]
the dreadful objects of the senses, as if full of poison,
secretly burn the heart.

**136.** All the senses one calls one's own —
not one gives up its cravings;
however much one feeds them with ever new things,
they will not turn back.

**137.** Greed, delusion, lust — none is weaker,
and with them pride, conceit and anger;
having stolen the mind, they lie hidden in it
and are never calmed for a moment.

**138.** Outwardly maya binds and whirls [the soul] about,
[giving] it a footing in caste and lineage;
forgetting Krishna, it wanders,
rooted in sinful, evil desires.

**139.** Everything wondrous you see in the world —
all of it hides Krishna;
human birth has meaning only then
when one serves Sri Krishna.

**140.** Human birth, as is known, is rare —
[it is given] for serving Krishna;
yet, having obtained such a body, people abandon Sri Krishna
and die in this false samsara.

**141.** Listen, all — I have told you what is in My heart:
bless Me,
that I may have love for Krishna and this sorrow may flee —
this boon I ask of everyone.

**142.** To sing Krishna's deeds unceasingly —
this is what My lips long for;
My eyes — on His lotus face,
My heart I shall bind to His holy feet.

**143.** What shall I say of My heart? Not seeing Krishna,
the fire of separation burns in its depths;
fallen into the ocean of samsara, into the deep,
My mind is distraught.

**144.** He alone is father and mother, he alone is a deity,
he alone a guru and friend,
who lets one hear and tells of Krishna,
who brings one to serve Krishna's feet.

**145.** You are My friends, supreme Vaishnavas:
do not withdraw your mercy from Me in your hearts.
I shall take sannyasa and spread prema —
all for your own good."

**146.** Having said this, Vishvambhara
rolls on the ground;
Gaura's golden body is grey with dust,
His loosened hair sweeps the ground.

**147.** "Haribol!" He calls aloud,
breathing deep and fast;
His body thrills from feet to head,
His speech falters, words left half-said.

**148.** Now He weeps, now He is in anguish,
now He starts and looks about,
now He gasps and tosses, His whole body trembling,
now He springs up in separation from Krishna.

**149.** Now He cries aloud: "Vrindavana!"
now He calls: "Radha!"
He slaps His arms like a wrestler, says "Hari, Hari",
now He strikes His breast with His hand.

**150.** Seeing this, all ponder within themselves,
anguished at heart:
what more is there to say? — it was as if they had fallen
into an ocean of grief.

**151.** Murari says: "Listen, Gaurahari:
You are independent in everything;
to teach people, to reveal mercy,
You take on the bhava of the pain of separation.[^36-2]

**152.** Whatever You do by the will of Your own heart —
what can others say about it?
You know everything; whatever You ordain —
what can poor souls do about it?

**153.** We are all jivas, we do not know what will become of us,
we are like worms and ants;
You are the ocean of mercy, the friend of all people:
consider and do what is fitting."

**154.** Hearing this, the Lord smiled
and embraced them all;
revealing prema, addressing them all,
He speaks words of consolation:

**155.** "Listen, all, to what I say,
let no one doubt:
wherever I may go, I remain with you —
know this."[^36-3]

**156.** Then Vishvambhara, taking leave of all,
went home;
with sannyasa in His heart He makes every preparation,
but His mother does not know of it.

**157.** Shachi's heart beats anxiously,
she finds no peace.
Lochana says: how can He wish
to leave such an ocean of love?

## Mother Shachi's Grief

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* Hearing from people that Sriman Mahaprabhu would soon take sannyasa, mother Shachi, beside herself with grief, weeping, earnestly begged the Lord not to take sannyasa in His youth but to keep the dharma of a householder. Then Sriman Mahaprabhu, telling [her the story] of Dhruva and of the instruction his mother gave him — to serve Krishna — [revealed to her] that apart from Krishna the living being has no other refuge; that therefore whoever teaches one to serve Krishna is one's true father, mother, brother and friend; that those intoxicated by the conceit of "I" and "mine" are the greatest fools; and that service to Krishna is the meaning of human life. Having declared this, He instructed her to give up attachment to her son such as souls in the grip of maya have, and vowed that, not like other sons [who bring their mothers] silver, gold and other perishable things, He would bring her the eternal, highest good — prema. Then Gaurahari showed Himself to her in the form of Sri Krishna and dispelled mother Shachi's grief.[^36-4]

*Raga ahiri; disha: "Ah, my son, do not leave me — but for you I have no one in the three worlds!"*

**1.** Thus, by guesses and rumours,
mother Shachi heard that her son would take sannyasa.

**2.** It was as if the sky had fallen on her head:
Shachi lost consciousness, her heart swooned.

**3.** Like a madwoman Shachi rushes about on every side;
whomever she sees she questions — all over Navadvipa.

**4.** Knowing for certain that her son would take sannyasa,
she came to Vishvambhara and heaves deep sighs:

**5.** "You are my only son, the one eye in my body:
when I do not see you, I see everything in darkness.

**6.** I hear from people, my child, that you will take sannyasa —
it is as if the sky had broken over my head.

**7.** I am alone, unprotected — I have no one else;
forgetting all else, I look only at your face.

**8.** You are the pupil of my eyes, the lamp of our family;
for having such a son as you Navadvipa calls me fortunate.

**9.** Do not destroy my pride, my son:
if you are gone, everything will go to ruin.

**10.** People counted it good fortune to see my face —
now, seeing me, they will turn away.

**11.** Having such a son as you, I am blessed in this world —
but not seeing you, I see nothing but wilderness around me.

**12.** Giving grief to this unfortunate woman, you will go away —
and I shall enter the Ganga and die.

**13.** How will you walk on such tender feet?
Hungry and thirsty — from whom will you beg food?

**14.** Your body is like a doll of butter: it will melt in the sun;
how will this wretched mother bear it?

**15.** Son of a mother bereft of her children, my golden Nimai!
Leaving me, where will you go?[^36-5]

**16.** I shall take poison and die before your eyes,
so as not to hear with my ears of your sannyasa.

**17.** Having killed me, my child, you will go to foreign lands —
and I shall light a fire and enter it.

**18.** To all beings you are merciful — but to me merciless;
I do not know why the Creator is so cruel to me.

**19.** In beauty, virtues and nature, my son, you are blessed in the three worlds;
your dress enchants women, your hair is lovely.

**20.** When you weave malati into your hair that falls to your shoulders,
my heart is eased at the sight of that attire.

**21.** You walk along the road surrounded by friends,
a book in your left hand — and seeing you, my heart is glad.

**22.** How will you leave your companions, my child?
Will you no longer perform sankirtana with them?

**23.** Will you no longer dance in such lovely attire,
at the sight of which the whole world is enchanted?

**24.** How will your dear ones live?
Your sannyasa will kill them all.

**25.** First I shall die, then Vishnupriya;
the devotees will die, their hearts bursting:

**26.** Murari, Mukunda Datta and Srinivasa,
Advaita Acharya and others, and Haridasa,

**27.** Gadadhara, Narahari, Sri Raghunandana,
Vasudeva Ghosha, Vakreshvara, Sri Rama —

**28.** all will die, not seeing you;
seeing all this, my child, soften your heart.

**29.** You are a son without a father; I married you twice,
but I have seen no offspring from it.

**30.** Sannyasa in youth is not dharma;
live in the householder's ashrama and fulfil all your duties.

**31.** In youth lust, anger, greed and delusion are strong —
how will your sannyasa succeed?

**32.** In the Kali age the mind cannot be curbed;
through the mind's unsteadiness the dharma of sannyasa perishes.

**33.** A householder is not bound by sins of the mind,
but a sannyasi's dharma is lost if his mind is defiled by desire."[^36-6]

**34.** When Shachi-devi had spoken these words,
[the Lord], having heard them, spoke words of consolation to His mother:

**34+1.** Narahari's lotus feet are the ornament of my head;
of Gauranga's deeds this servant Lochana tells[^36-7].

*Raga — any; refrain: "The nectar words of the moon-like lips…"[^36-8]*

**35.** "Dhruva's mother made Dhruva a Vaishnava —
I shall tell that rasa: hear a wondrous tale.[^36-9]

*Thus [in the Padyavali]:*

**36.** What was the conduct of the hunter, what the age of Dhruva, what the learning of Gajendra, what the name and beauty of Kubja, what the wealth of Sudama,
what the lineage of Vidura, what the prowess of Ugrasena, lord of the Yadavas? Madhava, who loves devotion, is pleased by devotion alone, not by qualities.[^36-10]

**37.** Listen, mother, attentively to the story of Dhruva —
how Dhruva attained the very highest position.

**38.** The mind-born son of Brahma was Svayambhuva Manu,
of great might and valour, like a second Brahma.

**39.** He had two sons, Priyavrata and Uttanapada;
both became great kings by Brahma's grace.

**40.** Maharaja Uttanapada married twice —
two beauties named Suruchi and Suniti.

**41.** Suruchi had seven sons, beginning with Uttama;
from Suniti's womb only Dhruva was born.[^36-11]

**42.** Uttama's mother won her husband's favour,
while Dhruva's mother fell out of favour with her husband.

**43.** The beautiful Suruchi became the chief queen,
and Dhruva's mother went to wait on her.

**44.** The grief of Dhruva's mother cannot be told:
at such grief stones float and the ocean dries up.

**45.** Rice of coarse grain and unsalted vegetables —
that is what they gave Dhruva's mother to eat.

**46.** When Dhruva was five years old,
Dhruva's unhappy mother suffered all kinds of hardship.

**47.** One day the Maharaja with Suruchi
sat in pleasure on a high throne.

**48.** The seven brothers, beginning with Uttama, with their father and mother,
were on the jewelled throne, enjoying all kinds of amusements.

**49.** Five-year-old Dhruva with other children,
grey with dust, was playing all kinds of games.

**50.** Seeing his seven brothers on his father's lap,
Dhruva climbs up onto the jewelled throne.

**51.** As he climbed onto the throne to get onto his father's lap,
Dhruva's stepmother pushed him down to the ground.

**52.** Fallen on the ground, Dhruva began to cry;
the king, in his wife's power, said nothing.

**53.** Fallen on the ground, Dhruva weeps in wounded pride:
he does not know that his mother is out of favour with his father.

**54.** Dhruva's stepmother says: "You cry for nothing:
the son of a maidservant — and you climb onto the jewelled throne!

**55.** Birth after birth your mother has not served Krishna —
have you no shame, climbing onto the jewelled throne?

**56.** Son of an unfortunate woman, your mother is no Vaishnavi:
where will you ever sit on a jewelled throne?"

**57.** When Dhruva's stepmother had said this,
Dhruva went weeping to his mother.

**58.** He told his mother: "Stepmother hit me,
she pushed me off the throne.

**59.** Stepmother says: 'Your mother does not serve Krishna —
are you not ashamed to sit on the jewelled throne?'

**60.** And I have heard one more strange thing:
all this time I did not know that you are her maidservant."

**61.** Hearing this, Dhruva's mother weeps:
"I did not serve Krishna, my child — I am unfortunate.

**62.** Birth after birth I never once thought of Krishna;
I am Krishna's servant, yet I did not serve Him.

**63.** Do not cry, do not cry, child, son of an unfortunate woman:
your stepmother taunts you, calling you a maidservant's son."

**64.** Weeping, the mother speaks words of comfort to Dhruva;
Lochana Dasa happily sings of Gora's qualities.

*Raga sindhura*

**65.** "From the womb of an unfortunate woman, my son Dhruva,
you were born: I did not serve Krishna.
You are not your father's darling, yet you want to climb onto the throne —
poor child, better you had not been born [to me]!

**66.** Do not cry, do not cry, Dhruva, I shall tell you what I know —
listen, listen to my words:
your stepmother once worshipped Krishna —
that is why she became fortunate.

**67.** If you serve Krishna's feet, what need have you of the throne?
Whatever you desire, you will obtain.
Give up empty pride, serve Krishna's feet —
and you will easily obtain everything.

**68.** Such is my son — and the whole world taunts us —
how will you climb onto your father's lap?
I was born unfortunate" — saying this, the queen
was drowned in tears.

**69.** "O Dhruva, listen, listen to my words:
no one else can free you from your grief
but the one Lotus-eyed.

**70.** Brahma and all the gods, by serving Krishna much,
obtained high places in heaven;
if you serve Krishna, what is a throne? —
you will be honoured in the three worlds."

**71.** Hearing his mother's words, Dhruva ponders within:
"Where shall I find Krishna?"
"You will find Krishna in Madhuvana; how to go there
I shall teach you.

**72.** If you are [truly] the son of Uttanapada
and wish to obtain that throne —
then [Krishna] will make you fortunate,
and you will gain that throne."[^36-12]

**73.** Placing the dust of his mother's feet on his head as an ornament,
at an auspicious hour he sets out,
fixing his mind on Sri Krishna's feet —
and in heaven cries of "Victory!" ring out.

*Raga suhai; refrain: "Teach me — where shall I go to find news of my dark Friend?"*

**74.** Hear, all of you, another wondrous story:
the Lord tells it, mother Shachi listens.

**75.** Honouring with his head the dust of his mother's feet,
weeping and weeping, he consoles his mother.

**76.** The great soul Dhruva set out for Madhuvana,
having set his heart on the high position — devotion to Krishna.

**77.** When Dhruva, weary from the road, suffered from hunger,
he would suddenly find sweet ripe fruits.

**78.** When Dhruva went on, tormented by thirst,
he would find fragrant water on the way.

**79.** Seeing this, all the gods are amazed:
"We do not know whose place this Dhruva will take."[^36-13]

**80.** On the way Narada met Dhruva
and began to speak sweet words to him:

**81.** "At the age for play you, a king's son,
needlessly harbour hurt and pride in your heart.

**82.** It is not the time for you now to go to the forest;
in old age serve Govinda, the treasury of virtues."

**83.** Dhruva answers: "If one is to serve Krishna in old age —
then if one dies in youth, what of that rule?"

**84.** Hearing this, the great sage rejoiced
and imparted to Dhruva the twelve-syllable mantra.

**85.** [Dhruva said:] "Before, I did not serve Krishna — hence so much grief;
my heart is pierced by the arrows of my stepmother's words.

**86.** You are most merciful, and I am unfortunate:
remove my grief by instructing me [in the service] of Krishna.

**87.** By the power of serving Krishna I shall gain such a place
as neither my father nor my grandfather attained."

**88.** [Narada said:] "Go, Dhruva, to Madhuvana, on the bank of the Kalindi;
make a firm seat, sit and remain unmoving.

**89.** Make the great seed-mantra your support:
'Om namo bhagavate vasudevaya'.

**90.** Always repeat this mantra —
and within seven days you will have realisation."

**91.** Having received initiation and instruction, Dhruva rejoiced
and, bowing, went to Vrindavana.

**92.** In some days he reached Madhuvana;
seeing the wish-fulfilling trees, he cast off ignorance.

**93.** The son of Uttanapada reaches Madhuvana —
in joy Lochana Dasa sings of Gora's qualities.

[^36-1]: The Lord speaks in the person of a man, to instruct people: He Himself is not subject to maya; "samsara" and "maya" in vv. 135–140 are the lot of conditioned souls. So Murari too understands it in v. 151.
[^36-2]: That is, the Lord, the independent Master, takes on the bhava of separation from Krishna in order to teach people and bestow His mercy on them.
[^36-3]: Cf. Murari 2.18.23: "I Myself always dwell in your shrine."
[^36-4]: The OCR of the summary is badly garbled (vv. 15–16 of the poem have also strayed into it); gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface ("the grief of mother Shachi on hearing that the Lord will take sannyasa, … the request [to keep] the [householder's] dharma; the Lord, telling the story of Dhruva, instructs in the service of Krishna; consoling His mother with various words and showing her the form of Sri Krishna, He dispels her grief"). The summary also covers pastimes told in the next portion. The disha (refrain) is given from the 1903 edition (it is illegible in the OCR of A); the 1983 edition has: "Ah, Nimai, my Nimai, do not leave me — but for you I have no one in all the world."
[^36-5]: Before Vishvarupa's birth Shachi had lost eight daughters, and Vishvarupa had taken sannyasa. In A this couplet and the beginning of the next have strayed into the OCR of the summary; restored from B and C.
[^36-6]: Shachi's arguments are the usual objections to sannyasa in youth and in the Kali age; the Lord answers them below. Shachi is the Lord's eternal mother (Yashoda in Vraja); her grief is the expression of a mother's love (vatsalya-prema).
[^36-7]: V. 34+1 (Lochana Dasa's signature) is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition has it in the main text, in brackets (as this editor marks lines found not in all manuscripts). In the 1983 edition the chapter ends with it, and the Lord's answer to His mother begins with the next chapter — with en/37, v. 180; the story of Dhruva (v. 35 — en/37, v. 179) is absent there (see note 8).
[^36-8]: The raga and refrain lines in A are badly corrupted ("যথা—রাগ । চান্দ-মুখের বচন অমিয়া । রূপ গল কেমন বিধি দৈরজ ধরিয়া ॥ ধ্রু ॥"); only the beginning is translated. In the 1903 edition these lines and the whole story of Dhruva (vv. 35–93 and beyond) are given in a footnote as an additional reading from one manuscript; the 1983 edition has no story of Dhruva: after v. 34 and the signature (v. 34+1) it goes straight on to the consolation of the mother. In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition the story is in the main text; we translate it but note that it is apparently a later insertion.
[^36-9]: The story of Dhruva is Bhagavata 4.8–9; Lochana Dasa retells it freely, with details not found in the Bhagavata.
[^36-10]: A shloka from Rupa Gosvami's Padyavali; the same as in en/24 (v. 15 of the section). Bhaktisiddhanta's prose rendering of the shloka is not translated.
[^36-11]: In the Bhagavata (4.8.8–9) Suruchi has one son, Uttama.
[^36-12]: The stanza is corrupt in A and in the 1903 edition ("তবে «'ননাম পরেশ তোমাকে মৌভাগ্য করে"; C: "তবে একবমাম ধরে? তোমাকে সৌভাগ্য করে"); translated by sense.
[^36-13]: The gods fear that the ascetic will take the place of one of them (cf. Bhagavata 4.8.80–81).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 14 (portion 37)

*The section "Mother Shachi's Grief" (end): Dhruva's austerities in Madhuvana and his testing by the gods; Narada with Krishna in Vaikuntha; Krishna grants Dhruva the highest place; Dhruva's return and reign; Shachi wishes to go with her son as a yogini; the Lord instructs His mother, shows her the form of Krishna and promises to come whenever she calls*

*Raga sindhura; disha: "Hari, O great one! I shall take refuge at Govinda's feet: the great sweetness of those crimson feet I have only now come to know."[^37-1]*

**94.** Seeing Madhuvana, Dhruva was filled with joy;
keeping the pilgrim's fast, he spent the night [there].

**95.** Bathing at dawn, Dhruva repeats the mantra;
he feels neither hunger nor thirst — and is awash in tears.

**96.** Once in five or seven days he ate a single badari berry,
and after a fortnight — a drop of water touched by tulasi.

**97.** Fasting thirty days at a stretch,
Dhruva broke his fast only once a month.[^37-2]

**98.** Arms raised, palms joined, standing on one foot,
Dhruva repeats the twelve-syllable mantra.

**99.** [Standing] in the waters of the Kalindi, feet upward,[^37-3] [he practised austerity],
and in the hot season he performed tapas amid fires blazing on four sides.

**100.** In winter he lies in the waters of the Kalindi,
in the rains on an open platform: so many hardships he endures.

**101.** Meditating and meditating, Dhruva entered samadhi:
the form of Tribhanga did not leave him until he saw Krishna.[^37-4]

**102.** Indra and the other gods were seized with wonder:
"We do not know whose place he will take!"

**103.** Brahma says: "What if he takes my place?
Dhruva will take Brahma's seat — a remedy must be found."

**104.** Kubera and Varuna say: "He will take my place:
Krishna will give it to him — we sense it."

**105.** Indra says: "Dhruva will take my place —
Krishnachandra in His mercy will give it to him at once."

**106.** Indra says: "My place is what everyone desires —
Dhruva will take it and leave me with nothing."

**107.** All the gods say: "I sit on a high throne;
Dhruva, the great ascetic, will take my place."

**108.** To break Dhruva's fierce austerity,
Brahma and the other gods devise various schemes.

**109.** Dhruva abides [in meditation on] Tribhanga with all his mind and heart;
Brahma, with Indra and the others, went to test him.

**110.** Right at Dhruva's ear someone shouts loudly:
"You have come here to your death, Dhruva!"

**111.** Another shouts: "Dhruva, your father is dead!"
Someone shouts: "Hey, Dhruva, a black cobra is coming!"

**112.** Another shouts: "Dhruva, your mother is dead!"
Someone shouts: "Dhruva, run away quickly!"

**113.** Another shouts: "Dhruva, a forest fire is coming!"
Someone shouts: "Ah, Dhruva is dead, dead!"

**114.** Indra brought an elephant, and it drove its tusks into Dhruva's chest;
coiling its trunk, it pulls at Dhruva's tongue.[^37-5]

**115.** Vayu, becoming a python, swallowed Dhruva;
Surya, taking the form of a tiger, drank Dhruva's blood.

**116.** Binding Dhruva with a serpent-noose, they threw him into fire;
Chandra drowned Dhruva in the waters of the Kalindi.

**117.** He on whose tongue the name of Krishna resounds —
what can the bites of countless serpents do to him?

**118.** Unable to break his meditation on Tribhanga,
Brahma and the other gods fled.

**119.** With all his mind Dhruva meditates on the Lord's feet;
joyfully this Lochana Dasa sang of [His] qualities.

*Raga — any; disha: "I have taken refuge at the crimson feet, O Gopala, merciful to the lowly."*

**120.** Your name is Purifier of the fallen;
glory, glory, glory to the Deliverer of the lowly!

**121.** Listen, all, to another wondrous tale:
I shall tell what Narada said to Krishna.

**122.** In Vaikuntha, on a jewelled throne, [Krishna] with Kamala —
the three listen to Narada's song to the vina.[^37-6]

**123.** With a gentle smile Krishna says to Narada:
"Why today is your mind not given to your song to the vina?"

**124.** Narada says: "Listen, Lotus-eyed One,
why my mind does not stay with my song to the vina:

**125.** Your devotee has stolen my mind.
You, Lord, at all times are [the refuge] of those who have nothing [but You] in their hearts."[^37-7]

**126.** Hearing Narada's words, the Lotus-eyed One [says]:
"Tell Me, which devotee is remembering [Me]?"

**127.** "The son of Uttanapada, [a boy] of great mind;
his mother Suniti is out of favour with her husband.

**128.** Dhruva's stepmother is named Suruchi;
she sits merrily with her husband on the throne.

**129.** The seven brothers, beginning with Uttama, with father and mother,
laugh and play merrily on the jewelled throne.

**130.** [Dhruva] saw the seven brothers on their father's lap —
and seeing this, Dhruva climbs onto the jewelled throne.

**131.** When he climbed the throne to get onto his father's lap,
Dhruva's stepmother pushed him down to the ground.

**132.** Falling to the ground, Dhruva began to weep;
and the king, in his wife's power, said nothing.

**133.** By his stepmother's words Dhruva fell into distress —
and [now] he performs austerities in Madhuvana, near the Kalindi."

**134.** Hearing Narada's words, the Lotus-eyed One
with a gentle smile speaks sweet words:

**135.** "To the uninitiated I do not show mercy:
the sins of the uninitiated I do not remove.[^37-8]

**136.** [But he] who has vowed to meditate on Me
is performing austerities in Madhuvana, having left mother and father.

**137.** From a Vaishnavi's womb a non-Vaishnava is never born;
when [the son] is a Vaishnava and [the mother] a Vaishnavi, all sorrow burns away.

**138.** The vow of a Vaishnava I shall surely fulfil:
whatever boon Dhruva desires, that boon I shall give.

**139.** My devotees have bound Me with the rope of prema and bhakti:
when a devotee remembers [Me], I cannot stay [away]."

**140.** Narada says: "Dhruva is not without initiation;
go and show him mercy — he is burning as in a forest fire."

**141.** Hearing [this] from Narada's lips, the Lotus-eyed One
came, riding on Garuda, to that Madhuvana.

**142.** With a gentle smile Krishna said to Dhruva:
"I have left Vaikuntha and come to give you a boon."

**143.** Hearing Sri Krishna's word, [Dhruva] was filled with joy;
coming out of meditation, he stood before Him with joined palms.

**144.** Dhruva says: "Great Lord, what boon shall I ask?
Be merciful to me — by that Your glory will grow."

**145.** The Lord says: "Your wish I shall surely fulfil:
whatever place you desire, that place I shall give.

**146.** Tell Me now, why have you come to Madhuvana?
[Is it because] your stepmother would not let you sit on the throne?

**147.** If I do not give you the very highest place,
how shall I bear the name 'Tree of Desires'?"

**148.** Dhruva says: "A high place I regard as a straw:
without devotion to You all is a heap of ashes."[^37-9]

**149.** Krishna says: "I shall give you [what is above] all thrones:
go and abide in the highest place in the three worlds.

**150.** Son of Uttanapada, you will be king;
all your subjects will come to know My glory.

**151.** Above all is the circle of the sages' abodes; [above it]
Dhruvaloka will stand" — so He told of everything.[^37-10]

**152.** Having given this boon, Krishna disappeared,
and Vishvakarma built Dhruvaloka.

**153.** Having received this boon, Dhruva set out;
joyfully this Lochana Dasa sings of Gora's qualities.

*Raga — any; disha: "Come, O Gaura-Gopala, my life!"*

**154.** Hearing Krishna's command, Dhruva set out for his homeland;
and here Uttanapada's detachment from the world grew.

**155.** Dhruva's stepmother weeps: "Where has Dhruva gone?
I, wretched one, pushed the son [off the throne]!"

**156.** The king says: "It was my fate to destroy my son;
when shall I see Dhruva again?"

**157.** The king says: "Mother of Dhruva, you are the chief queen;
from today all your co-wives are your maidservants."

**158.** Not seeing his son, the king, distraught,
lies senseless on the ground.

**159.** Suddenly he saw Narada —
the maharaja rose, anxious at heart.

**160.** Offering water for the feet and arghya, he gave [Narada] a seat
and began to tell him what was in his heart:

**161.** "I had a five-year-old boy —
I do not know where that boy has gone."

**162.** Narada says: "Many troubles befell Dhruva;
having gained devotion to Krishna, he has come near [your] land.

*Thus [in the scriptures]:*

**163.** Holy is the family, fulfilled is the mother, blessed is the earth and the place where she dwells, blessed too are her forefathers abiding in heaven — [that mother] whose son is known in the world as a Vaishnava.

**164.** She whose son is a Vaishnava is truly called the mother of a son; but the mother of a hundred non-Vaishnava sons is like a sow.[^37-11]

**165.** In whose line even one Vaishnava is born,
[he] delivers the families of father, mother and father-in-law.

**166.** Your boy, having served Sri Krishna, is returning:
know that Dhruva is the ornament of your line."

**167.** At Narada's words the king, filled with joy,
sprinkled the royal road with chuya and sandal.

**168.** Curd, auspicious durva grass, kunkuma, musk,
delicate bright flowers; lamps burn in rows.

**169.** In search of his lost [son] the king hastens to meet him;
having gone some distance, he finds Dhruva.

**170.** Seeing Dhruva, the king seemed to regain his life;
covering his son with countless kisses, he took him in his arms.

**171.** Bringing Dhruva back, he made him king before all
and from hand to hand gave over to him the ministers and subjects.

**172.** Giving the kingdom to Dhruva, the king went to the forest;
[and Dhruva] ruled for a long time with a joyful heart.

**173.** By might and power he conquered many lands one after another
and ruled forty years free of enemies.[^37-12]

**174.** Having shown much valour among the gods and gandharvas,
Dhruva went with his mother to Dhruvapuri."

**175.** Shachi says: "I too shall go with you,
I shall stay with you — in the joy of talks about Krishna.

**176.** If such a golden son as you goes away with a shaven head,
I too shall shave my head and become a shaven-headed woman.

**177.** I shall put on red clothes, put kundala earrings in my ears,
become a yogini and go with you."[^37-13]

**178.** At His mother's words the Lord was at a loss
and began to think: "How shall I console her?"

**179.** The crest-jewel of the all-knowing, Shachi's son,
consoles His mother, [sings] this Lochana Dasa.

*Raga varadi; disha: "This wondrous tale is called 'a blessing to the ear': listen to the song of Gora's qualities, Shachi's darling moon!"*

**180.** "Do not be troubled, listen to My words:
why do you needlessly torment your heart with grief?

**181.** Again and again I tell you — and you do not heed:
in vain you [give yourself to] greed, delusion, anger and pride.

**182.** Who are you, who is your son, who is whose father?
In vain you lament, saying 'yours' and 'mine'.

**183.** What is woman, what is man, who is whose husband? —
apart from Sri Krishna's feet there is no other refuge.

**184.** He is father, He is mother, He is friend and kin;
He is the destroyer, He the creator, He the only wealth.

**185.** Without Him all is empty — this truth I have told you:
without Him empty is all there is in the world.[^37-14]

**186.** All people are firmly bound by the bonds of Vishnu's maya
and suffer only from their own intoxication and conceit.

**187.** Whatever deeds one does, thinking, 'This is good for me' —
those very dharmas hold one captive in the hereafter.

**188.** Captive on the thread of karma, one wanders and roams;
the fool, forgetting Krishna, does not know himself.

**189.** Among the fourteen worlds birth as a human being
is known to be rare — this secret I have revealed to you.

**190.** The fruits of sense enjoyment in this [world] are countless,
and this transient samsara itself crumbles in a moment.

**191.** And yet the human body is known to be rare
[for one] who serves Krishna, standing firm against maya.

**192.** All these bodies are only for the service of Sri Krishna;
he is freed from bondage who loves Krishna.

**193.** With what great tenderness you love Me as a son! —
if [that love] were given to Sri Krishna's feet, how great would be the gain!

**194.** Attachment to samsara [leads only] to death,
but attachment to Sri Krishna — to crossing [the ocean of] birth.

**195.** He alone is the highest friend, he alone mother and father,
who gives prema-bhakti to Sri Krishna's feet.

**196.** My heart is in anguish in separation from Krishna;
falling at your feet, I humbly answer you.

**197.** You have given Me boundless love;
by your command I purify My heart.

**198.** My deliverance is your salvation too:
serve Sri Krishna's feet, give up the thought 'he is my son'.[^37-17]

**199.** I shall take sannyasa for the sake of prema for Krishna —
and from distant lands I shall bring and give you the wealth of prema.

**200.** Other people's sons bring silver and gold:
spend it, and it is gone; this is not the highest dharma.

**201.** They earn wealth and bring it with great hardship —
and then either the wealth goes, or [the earner] himself dies.

**202.** But I shall bring you such wealth as prema for Krishna:
Krishna's feet are the sum of all treasures.

**203.** Prema is imperishable in this world and the next.
Give Me leave, mother, and calm [the grief] in your heart.[^37-15]

**204.** Father and mother everyone obtains in every birth,
but Krishna and the guru are not obtained — understand this in your heart.

**205.** Only in human birth can all come to know Krishna and the guru;
one who does not accept a guru I regard as a beast or a bird."

**206.** Hearing this, Shachi-devi, wonder-struck at heart,
gazes unblinking at Vishvambhara's lotus face.

**207.** The Lord of the fourteen worlds removed maya [from her]:
Shachi sees all beings as alike, equal.

**208.** At that very moment she saw Krishna in Vishvambhara:
maya — [the thought] 'this is my son' — went away.[^37-16]

**209.** His body dark, outshining a fresh cloud,
thrice-bent, holding the flute, in splendid yellow garments —

**210.** with cowherds, gopis and cowherd boys in Vrindavana
she then saw her own son — and stood amazed.

**211.** Seeing this, Shachi was astonished at heart;
her body thrilled, its hairs on end, her whole frame trembling.

**212.** [Yet] in her tenderness Shachi does not give up her own relation [to Him]:
"Krishna has become my son — so my good fortune has decreed.

**213.** Krishna, unattainable for the world, is my son;
He is subject to no one's control — what can my strength do?"

**214.** Reasoning thus, Shachi spoke:
"You are the independent Lord, the jewel among men.

**215.** By my good fortune You were so long in my power —
now, as it pleases You, take sannyasa.

**216.** I have one request of You:
why is such a treasure of mine going away?"

**217.** Saying this, she spoke in a piteous voice;
the tears streamed from her eyes.

**218.** Sobbing and sobbing weeps virtuous Shachi;
at His mother's weeping the Lord bowed His head.

**219.** Then, raising His face again, Vishvambhara says:
"Listen, mother, to My answer:

**220.** on any day when you long with love to see Me —
at that very moment you will be able to see Me."[^37-18]

**221.** Hearing these words, Shachi holds back her tears;
with an aching heart [this] was told by this Lochana Dasa.

[^37-1]: The disha is corrupt in A (OCR: "হরিএ মহাশয় … ও-রাজ।চ্ণের অনেক মাতরী এলে নে জানিলুং") and is restored from the 1903 edition (C). Vv. 94–179 are absent from the 1983 edition (B), and in C they stand in the same footnote as the beginning of the story of Dhruva (see en/36, note 8): apparently a later insertion.
[^37-2]: Lit. "performs parana (the breaking of the fast) once a month". The line is corrupt in A, restored from C.
[^37-3]: So in A and C. Lochana Dasa's account of Dhruva's austerities does not follow the Bhagavata (4.8.71–76), where Dhruva gradually gives up fruits, leaves, water and finally breath, standing on one foot.
[^37-4]: Tribhanga ("thrice-bent") is Krishna of Vrindavana with the flute. In Lochana Dasa Dhruva meditates on this form, while the Lord comes to him from Vaikuntha on Garuda (vv. 141–142); in the Bhagavata Narada teaches Dhruva to meditate on the four-armed form (4.8.44–52). Lochana Dasa calls the Lord of Vaikuntha Krishna: Krishna is the source of all His manifestations.
[^37-5]: The tongue that repeats the mantra (cf. v. 117). The gods' trials are Lochana Dasa's detail; in the Bhagavata the gods only suffocate from Dhruva's austerity and beg the Lord for protection (4.8.80–82).
[^37-6]: Kamala is Lakshmi. The third listener is not named — apparently Bhu-devi, Narayana's other consort.
[^37-7]: The line is obscure ("মনের দরিদ্র নাথ তুমি সর্বকাল", lit. "of the poor in mind Lord You are at all times"); translated by the probable sense: the Lord is the treasure of the humble, who have nothing but Him.
[^37-8]: The second line is corrupt in A and C (A: "অপরাত নহি ঘন", C: "অপরাধ নাহি ধার"); translated by sense. The thought concerns the importance of diksha: Narada answers (v. 140) that Dhruva is initiated (he himself gave him the mantra, en/36, vv. 84–90).
[^37-9]: Cf. Bhagavata 4.9.35: there Dhruva only after seeing the Lord laments that he desired what is transient; in Lochana Dasa he at once places devotion above every station.
[^37-10]: Dhruvaloka is the Pole Star, above the circle of the seven rishis (the Great Bear); cf. Bhagavata 4.9.20–25.
[^37-11]: Two Sanskrit shlokas; Bhaktisiddhanta adds a prose rendering of them (not translated). The second shloka is garbled by the OCR of A; restored from C and from Bhaktisiddhanta's rendering ("she whose son is a Vaishnava is truly the mother of a son").
[^37-12]: In the Bhagavata Dhruva reigns thirty-six thousand years (4.12.13).
[^37-13]: Vv. 175–179 are absent in B; in C they stand in the same footnote as the story of Dhruva. In place of en/36, v. 35 — en/37, v. 179 B has only a signature couplet (en/36, v. 34+1). Red (saffron) clothes and earrings are the garb of wandering yogis. Shachi's words are an outburst of a mother's prema: she cannot conceive of life apart from her son.
[^37-14]: "Michha" (lit. "false") here means "empty, vain": whatever is unconnected with Krishna brings no good. This is not the mayavada doctrine of the world's unreality: the world is real but transient (v. 190), and what is true in it is service to Krishna.
[^37-15]: The word before "মা" ("mother") is unclear (A: "নেদলী", C: "বেধনী", B: "के दो"); translated by sense.
[^37-16]: Maya here is that which hides from Shachi her son's divinity: it is yogamaya, which nourishes her motherly love (vatsalya), not a veil of ignorance — Shachi is the Lord's eternal mother (cf. en/36, note 6). The Lord lifts this covering for a moment, but, as v. 212 says, even after the vision Shachi does not give up her motherly feeling. Cf. Yashoda seeing the universe in Krishna's mouth (Bhagavata 10.8.43–45).
[^37-17]: Vv. 196–198: the Lord speaks in the role of a devotee seeking Krishna (cf. en/36, note 1); He Himself is bound by nothing and needs no purification. "My deliverance (nistara)" — by letting Him go [to sannyasa], His mother will herself attain salvation.
[^37-18]: Cf. the Lord's promise to the devotees (en/36, v. 155) and to Srivasa (Murari 2.18.23).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 15 (portion 38)

*The section "Vishnupriya's Lament": Vishnupriya learns of the coming sannyasa and begs the Lord not to leave; the Lord consoles her, instructs her and shows her His four-armed form; Srivasa asks to be taken along; in Murari's house the Lord tells him to worship Advaita. The section "The Consolation of the Devotees" (only in the 1983 edition): the last days at home and the last night with Vishnupriya*

## Vishnupriya's Lament

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* [When mother Shachi could not tell] her of this, Vishnupriya-devi, beside herself with grief, wept unceasingly, and [the Lord] asked her the reason for her tears. Vishnupriya-devi laid her grief at the Lord's feet; [He], [learning] the cause of her grief, consoled her with many sweet words and at last, giving her full instruction in the truth — [that it is vain] to see one's husband in a body of blood and flesh [and that the true Lord of the soul's life is Sri Hari], — showed Vishnupriya-devi His four-armed form of Narayana. Then Srivasa, Murari and other devotees, hearing in anxiety the news that the Lord would take sannyasa, came to Him, poured out their grief and asked to go with the Lord; Gaurasundara, giving them instruction in the truth, consoled them.[^38-1]

*Raga varadi, the "dhulakhela" tune*

**1.** Then queen Shachi-devi [wishes] to tell what is in her heart —
her face is wan with grief;
but no word comes from her lips, tears stream from her eyes —
seeing this, Vishnupriya fell senseless.

**2.** She cannot ask; deep in her heart is pain;
she had heard people's whispers —
and by hints understood the matter: a thunderbolt fell out of season,
and the poor one lost her senses.

**3.** Vishnupriya ponders in her heart. At the close of day the Lord
came home in haste;
having eaten and drunk, He lay down contentedly on His bed —
and Vishnupriya hurried [to Him].

**4.** Sighing, she sits by His lotus feet
and gazes [at Him] with a sorrowful face;
laying on her bosom the feet of her beloved Lord of life,
she binds them with her creeper-arms.

**5.** Tears flow from both her eyes, the cloth over her bosom is soaked,
streams run down [the Lord's] feet.
Feeling it, the Lord suddenly rises
and asks His beloved what is wrong.[^38-2]

**6.** "You are dear to Me as life — why do you weep? I want to know:
answer Me, beloved."
Setting her on His lap, taking her chin in His right hand,
He asks in sweet words.

**7.** Vishnupriya-devi weeps, her heart is bursting;
when asked, she says not a word;
life smoulders within her, her body is senseless —
only tears stream from her eyes.

**8.** Again and again the Lord asks — and still she does not answer,
but only weeps, clasping His feet.
The Lord, who knows every art, asks in many ways,
wiping her face with the edge of His cloth.

**9.** He begins one playful talk after another, awakening feeling in her —
at such words even a stone would blossom.
Seeing the Lord's eagerness, moon-faced Vishnupriya
spoke in a faltering voice:

**10.** "Listen, listen, Lord of my life, put Your hand on my head [and tell the truth]:
are You really going to take sannyasa?
Hearing it from people, I feel my heart will burst —
I shall enter fire.

**11.** For You are my life and wealth, my beauty and youth,
my dress, my graces, feelings and arts;
when You go away, what use is this wretched life? —
my heart burns as with poison.

**11+1.** There is no other young woman as fortunate as I:[^38-3]
You are my beloved Lord of life;
great were my love and hope — I gave myself to You,
[trusting] that You would give me Your hand in this youth.

**12.** Shame on my body! One thing I ask You:
how will You walk the roads on foot?
Your feet are tender as shirisha flowers —
my hand is afraid to touch them.

**13.** When You stand on the ground, my heart quakes with fear
and my whole body shudders;
in the forests, among thorns, where will You go, to what lands?
How will You walk on Your crimson feet?

**14.** On Your nectar moon-face drops of sweat appear
at the slightest effort;
in the rainy season — now downpour, now scorching heat:
sannyasa will bring [You] great suffering.

**15.** Apart from Your feet I know nothing —
to whom will You leave me?
Have You no fear of [breaking] dharma? Shachi is old and half-dead —
how will You leave such a mother?

**16.** Murari, Mukunda Datta — such devotees,
Srinivasa and Haridasa,
Advaita Acharya and the others — leaving them, what will You achieve?
How can You take sannyasa?

**17.** You, Lord, are a treasury of prema — so the whole world thinks —
yet Your deeds and intentions are the opposite.
You will go to far lands — hearing it, everyone will die,
and all will be covered with disgrace.

**18.** What shall I, worthless one, say? If I am the root of Your household life
and You take sannyasa because of me —
let me take Your misfortunes upon myself and die by taking poison,[^38-4]
and You live happily in Your own home.

**19.** Lord, do not go to far lands: I have no one in this world;
when I look at Your face my heart burns."
She can say no more — deep in her heart is pain;
she only weeps, clasping His feet.

**20.** Hearing Vishnupriya's words, then that jewel Gauranga
smiling, took her onto His lap;
wiping her face with His cloth, He jests in many ways
and says: "Do not dwell on needless grief.

**21.** That I will leave you and go to take sannyasa —
who ever told you that?
Whatever I do, when I do it, I shall tell you then —
but now do not die of needless sorrow."

**22.** Saying this, Gaurahari embraces and kisses her
in many sweet and playful pastimes;
endless are the delights of His prema, He is the limit of grace in lila —
so in every way He comforted Vishnupriya.[^38-5]

**23.** In joyful play the night came to an end,
and Vishnupriya asks again:
there is fire in her heart — that is why she asks again,
gazing at the face of her beloved Lord of life.

**24.** Pressing the Lord's hand to her breast, Vishnupriya-devi asks:
"Do not lie to me for fear of me.
This is what I think: all You say is mere playfulness —
You will run away without my knowing.

**25.** You, Lord, are Your own master, never in another's power,
and You do what pleases You.
If You will take sannyasa — what can I say?
[Only] tell me the truth."

**26.** Hearing these words, the Lord smiled softly
and says: "Listen, My beloved,
do not grieve at all in your heart: what I say for your good
listen to attentively.

**27.** All that you see in the world count as empty:
Bhagavan alone is true,
and true are the Vaishnavas; all else besides them
count as empty.[^38-6]

**28.** Empty are son, husband, wife, father, mother — all that goes by such names:
in the end, who belongs to whom?
Apart from Sri Krishna's feet there is no other kin;
all that you see is His maya.

**29.** Whether woman or man — see: the soul of all is one [in nature];
only in the bonds of maya does one imagine 'two'.
Sri Krishna is the husband of all, and all others are prakriti:
this no one understands.[^38-7]

**30.** From the union of blood and seed a person is born in a place of filth
and falls to the ground knowing nothing;
as a child, a youth, an old man, he suffers many miseries —
and takes pride in his body and home.

**31.** Those he raised as his own all revile him;
in pride he lives out his old age;
hearing and sight grow dim, he weeps thinking of his woes —
and still he does not serve Govinda.

**32.** He took a body in this world in order to serve Krishna —
yet in the bonds of maya he forgets himself;
drunk with conceit, forgetting his own Lord,
in the end he dies in the torments of hell.

**33.** Your name is Vishnupriya ('dear to Vishnu'): make it meaningful,
do not keep needless grief in your heart.
I have told you this — put away other thoughts
and give your mind to the deeds of Krishna."

**34.** Being Himself the Lord, He removed His own maya,[^38-8] —
and Vishnupriya's heart grew clear:
grief and sorrow went away, her breast filled with joy —
and suddenly she sees [Him] four-armed.

**35.** Yet even seeing the four-armed form,
Vishnupriya-devi does not give up thinking of Him as her husband;
falling at His feet, she bows and pleads:
"Hear, Lord, one request of mine.

**36.** I, the lowest and most worthless, was born in this world,
and You are my beloved husband, the Lord of my life;
such was my good fortune — I had become Your maidservant —
why then [now] such a fall?"

**37.** Saying this, Vishnupriya wept aloud,
and her distress grew greater still.
Seeing His beloved's anguish, the Lord, His eyes brimming with tears,
took her in His arms and bestowed His grace:

**38.** "Listen, Vishnupriya-devi, this is what I tell you:
whenever you think of Me —
wherever I may go, I am with you;
truly, truly I tell you firmly."[^38-9]

**39.** Hearing the word of Krishna's command, Vishnupriya thought:
"This Lord is the independent Master,
He does what pleases Him — who can stand in His way?" —
and she made no reply.

**40.** Vishnupriya lowered her face, her eyes brimming with tears;
seeing this, the Lord speaks tender words to her.
It is painful to hear of the Lord's deeds —
this Lochana Dasa sings of [His] qualities.

*Raga varadi; disha: "My life, O moon of the twice-born! The sweetness of the form of Gora, who enchants Madana, ever wakes [in my heart] — I would take that form's misfortunes on myself and die"*

**41.** Thus, in surmises, days and nights go by —
as if a fire had flared up in everyone's heart.

**42.** All the devotees, gathering together,
speak of Gora's qualities, weeping in the depths of their souls.

**43.** Shachi and Vishnupriya both weep day and night:
darkness on every side, all seems empty.

**44.** Townsfolk and household find no peace:
all wander restlessly about the town.

**45.** At this time Srinivasa, king of brahmanas,
with an aching heart asks the Lord:

**46.** "I have one request — I am afraid to say it:
if You permit, Lord, I shall go with You.

**47.** And whoever else can — let him go with You too:
not seeing You, no one will stay alive.

**48.** I shall die first, listen, Vishvambhara" —
and he laid open to [the Lord] what was in his heart.

**49.** Hearing this, the Lord laughed softly:
"Listen, Srinivasa, to what I say.

**50.** Do not fear separation from Me:
I shall never leave you all.

**51.** And especially in your house, in Krishna's shrine,
I abide always: set your mind at rest."[^38-10]

**52.** Having cheered him with words of comfort,
in the evening He went to the house of Murari Gupta.

**53.** Taking Haridasa with Him, [He came] to Murari's house
and speaks to him in private in the Deity's room:

**54.** "Listen, Murari, to My words:
you are dear to Me as life — that is why I speak.

**55.** I shall tell you something excellent — listen attentively:
I give you this instruction for your own good.

**56.** Advaita Acharya Gosani is blessed in the three worlds;
I have no other friend closer than He.

**57.** He Himself is a part of the Lord, the guru of all:[^38-11]
whoever wishes his own good, let him serve Him.

**58.** He is the benefactor of the world, the king of Vaishnavas:
worship Him with the highest devotion.

**59.** When He receives worship, Krishna receives it.
I have told you this in private — keep it in your heart.

**60.** I and Gadadhara Pandita Gosani,
Nityananda, Advaita, Srivasa, Ramai —

**61.** know that My body is one with all of them.[^38-12]
I have opened My heart to you — keep it in your heart."

**62.** Hearing this, Murari, king of physicians,
understood in his heart what the Lord intended in His.

**63.** Weeping, he fell at the Lord's feet:
he understood that the Lord would surely take sannyasa.

**64.** Bowing at Haridasa's feet,
he surrenders himself [to the Lord] with boundless humility.

**65.** The Lord cannot bear to hear Murari weep:
He hastily rose and went home.

**66.** He consoled Murari with these words:
"I am always near you.

**67.** My sannyasa is still some time off;
and what I have told you will be your support in the end."[^38-13]

**68.** Saying this, the Lord goes home;
with an aching heart Lochana sings of it.

## The Consolation of the Devotees

*Raga karunashri; disha: "Ah, alas, alas! The Lord at whose remembrance sorrow ends — ah, alas! If He goes, I shall die, O Gauranga; whose face shall I look upon then, O Gauranga?"*

**68+1.** The Lord spends the night with joy in His heart[^38-14]
and increases [His] love [for all] even more than before.

**68+2.** Knowing His mother's heart, He gladdens her
with such words as set her heart at ease.

**68+3.** With townsfolk and household — with each as is fitting —
thus He shows love to all.

**68+4.** Laying aside [His] absorption in renunciation,
the Lord reveals His love in every house.

**68+5.** In one house He jests and laughs —
He finds the way to each heart and charms everyone.

**68+6.** Those who had been [ready] in secret, in disguise, to go with Him —
by the power of [His] maya returned home.[^38-15]

**68+7.** He wears various ornaments, sandal paste on His holy body,
and every moment He is full of laughter, play and rasa.

**68+8.** Everyone concluded there would be no sannyasa,
and all the people [went] home at ease.[^38-16]

**68+9.** The Lord lay down in His chamber,
and Vishnupriya came with a bunch of betel in her hand.

**68+10.** Smiling with delight, the Lord says, "Come, come!" —
and with great tenderness seated her on His lap.

**68+11.** Vishnupriya anointed the Lord's body with sandal
and drew a tilaka with fragrant aguru and musk,

**68+12.** put a divine garland of malati on Gora's body
and with tender jests placed betel in His holy mouth.

**68+13.** Then Mahaprabhu, the crown of connoisseurs of rasa,
Himself began to adorn Vishnupriya.

**68+14.** Her long hair, whose lustre surpasses Kama's whisk,
He braided and decked with malati flowers —

**68+15.** as if the crescent moon had captured a cloud —
or is [the moon] swallowing it and giving it back? — one cannot tell.

**68+16.** On her lovely forehead He placed a dot of sindura —
as if the moon had taken the sun onto its lap.

**68+17.** And around the sindura, dots of sandal,
as if a host [of stars] had rushed to see the sun in the moon's lap.[^38-17]

**68+18.** On her eyes, restless as wagtails, He drew lines of collyrium
and made her brows the string of Kama's bow.

**68+19.** He anointed her bosom with fragrant aguru and musk
and fitted her a bodice of divine cloth.

**68+20.** He adorned her with various ornaments —
and plays [with her] endlessly, laughing and offering betel.

**68+21.** He gazes at her face, whose beauty is a wonder of the three worlds,
and lovingly kisses her lips, red as the bandhuli flower.

**68+22.** Now He enfolds her in His creeper-arms and embraces her —
like a young lotus in the trunk of a lordly elephant.

**68+23.** Many rasas the playful Nagara unfolds —
what of others: this is beyond the reach of Kama.[^38-18]

**68+24.** Like lightning flashing in the lap of Sumeru:
Madana himself is bewildered seeing this play of love.

**68+25.** He holds her on His breast and does not lay her on the bed;
they cannot turn from each other — the two are as one being.

**68+26.** Breast to breast, face to face they pass the night;
in the languor of rasa both fall blissfully asleep.

**68+27.** At the end of the night the Lord rose quickly;
Vishnupriya sleeps a deep sleep.

**68+28.** At the time of renunciation His prema surges higher still;
with the thought "I shall take sannyasa" His heart is as if maddened.

**68+29.** Why at such a time He unfolds play, rasa and bhava —
hear the reason, and what is gained by it.

**68+30.** As one serves, so the Lord responds to him:
He never repays service with more or with less.[^38-19]

**68+31.** But here there is a special distinction according to [the devotees'] qualification:
devotion is without guile and with guile, with knowledge and with detachment.[^38-20]

**68+32.** Without bhakti no one can serve Krishna;
[the highest] bhakti is that which is without guile and unwavering.

**68+33.** And when prema-bhakti is without anuraga,
no one can capture Krishna.

**68+34.** Such is Thakura Gaura, the ocean of mercy:
the friend of the lowly, He Himself begs for prema with anuraga.

**68+35.** In mercy He reveals His own anuraga,
so that in separation her bhava may grow in her heart.

**68+36.** "Whoever sees My body with bhava —
with him My bhava is never broken."

**68+37.** Who else is such a treasury of mercy,
who gives prema and anuraga so as to bind His very Self?

**68+38.** This is why [He showed] grace to Vishnupriya —
knowing this, let no one go astray in mind.[^38-21]

**68+39.** This prema-bhakti the Lord will reveal —
with joy in his heart says this Lochana Dasa.

[^38-1]: The OCR of the summary is badly garbled; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface ("Vishnupriya-devi's grief; the Lord consoles her with many sweet words and, after instructing her in the truth, shows the four-armed form of Narayana; the coming of Srinivasa, Murari and other devotees; the Lord consoles them"). Before v. 1 B (the 1983 edition) has the disha "Gauranga, oh, why did You come to Nadia? O Gauranga, Gauranga!"; A lacks it.
[^38-2]: The end of the line is unclear (A and B: "অভিপারা"); translated by sense.
[^38-3]: Stanza 11+1 is found only in the 1983 edition (not in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition or the 1903 edition). From here B's numbering is one higher than A's.
[^38-4]: Lit. "taking Your nichhani" — the rite in which a lover takes the beloved's misfortunes upon herself. The beginning of the stanza is corrupt in A and B; restored by sense.
[^38-5]: Vishnupriya is the Lord's eternal consort, His Bhu-shakti (en/21, note 9); her pastimes with the Lord are not mundane (en/35, note 8).
[^38-6]: "Empty" (মিছা, "false") in the sense of vain, transient; not the mayavada doctrine of the world's unreality (cf. en/37, note 14).
[^38-7]: Not monism: souls are one in nature (all are the Lord's energy, His prakriti) and in themselves neither male nor female; the imagined "two" is identification with the body. The one Purusha, the enjoyer, is Krishna.
[^38-8]: That is, the yogamaya that hid His divinity from Vishnupriya (cf. en/37, note 16).
[^38-9]: Cf. the promise to Shachi (en/37, v. 220).
[^38-10]: Cf. en/36, v. 155; Murari 2.18.19–23.
[^38-11]: Advaita is Maha-Vishnu (en/27, note 8); cf. Murari 2.18.29: "a part of the Lord". Vv. 52–61 — cf. Murari 2.18.25–30.
[^38-12]: Ramai is Sri Rama Pandita, Srivasa's brother. In the 1903 edition vv. 60–61 are bracketed as the reading of some manuscripts.
[^38-13]: In A v. 63 is misprinted "৬২", and the numbers of vv. 47 and 50 are not printed; restored from the neighbours. V. 66 is corrupt in A and restored from B and C.
[^38-14]: This section is not in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition (1922); it is translated from Haridasa Shastri's edition (1983), where it comes under the heading "The Consolation of the Devotees" (भक्तगणे प्रबोध) right after v. 68. The 1903 edition has it in the main text (some couplets bracketed as readings of some manuscripts). Bhaktisiddhanta apparently deliberately left out this description of the Lord's night with Vishnupriya, written in the spirit of nagari-bhava (cf. en/09, note 9): Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Vrindavana Dasa, see in the Gaura of Navadvipa a model of the strict propriety of a brahmana householder. We translate it under the rule on the additions of the 1983 edition; Lochana Dasa himself explains the meaning of this pastime in vv. 68+29–68+38.
[^38-15]: Maya here is the Lord's lila-shakti (yogamaya), which conceals His intention for the sake of the lila.
[^38-16]: So in C ("না হবে সন্ন্যাস"); B has "লহিব সন্ন্যাস" ("will take sannyasa"), which contradicts the sense.
[^38-17]: The forehead is the moon, the dot of sindura the sun, the dots of sandal the stars. The end of the line is corrupt in B ("রাশি কোলে"); one manuscript of the 1903 edition has "like stars in the lap of the sun".
[^38-18]: The reading is unclear (B: "আহক অনের কাজ", C: "অস্ুক আনের কাখ"); the sense is that what in others would be a matter of lust is here "beyond the reach of Kama": the love of the Lord and His shakti transcends mundane desire (cf. v. 68+38).
[^38-19]: Cf. Bhagavad-gita 4.11.
[^38-20]: The couplet is obscure ("অমায়া সমায়া ভক্তি সবেদ নির্বেদ"; C: "এ বেদ নির্বেদ"); translated by the probable sense.
[^38-21]: That is, let no one think that the Lord's night with Vishnupriya before sannyasa is mundane love: it is a grace by which the Lord deepens her prema in separation (v. 68+35).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 16 (portion 39)

*The section "The Lord's Sannyasa" (beginning): at dawn the Lord leaves Navadvipa and swims across the Ganga; the lament of Shachi, Vishnupriya and the devotees; Nityananda with Chandrashekhara and others follows the Lord; the Lord in Kantakanagara with Keshava Bharati; Bharati hesitates to give Him sannyasa, and the Lord whispers the sannyasa mantra into his ear; the grief of the people of Kantakanagara; the Lord asks for their blessing*

## The Lord's Sannyasa

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* [Sri Gaurasundara, having consoled the devotees] with instruction in the truth, [left home] and, in order to take sannyasa, crossed the Ganga and came to Katwa, to Keshava Bharati. [Mother Shachi and] Vishnupriya [were overwhelmed] in separation from the Lord; Nityananda Prabhu, consoling them, came with Chandrashekhara Acharya, Damodara Pandita and other devotees to the Lord in Kantakanagara. The Lord asked Keshava Bharati for sannyasa, but he would not agree to give Him sannyasa.
