Then Bhagavan Himself, [wishing to receive] the sannyasa mantra and knowing what was in Bharati's heart, on some pretext first spoke the sannyasa mantra into Keshava Bharati's ear, and then Bharati agreed to give Him sannyasa. All the inhabitants of Kantakanagara — children, old people, youths, women and men — lamented bitterly on seeing the Lord's sannyasa; Sri Gaurachandra consoled them and, [bearing Himself] as a devotee, asked them for [the blessing of] devotion to Krishna. [A voice from the sky proclaimed the name "Sri Krishna Chaitanya": the Lord] awakened [all to Krishna] — therefore His name in sannyasa became "Sri Krishna Chaitanya". Then, having taken sannyasa, Sriman Mahaprabhu, absorbed in prema, wandered for three days in Radha-desha.[^39-1]

*Raga karunashri; disha: "O Lord, O Gora, alas! O Gorachand, alas!"*

**1.** Rising at dawn and performing His morning duties,
Gaurahari settled His resolve: "I shall take sannyasa."

**2.** "In Kantakanagara lives Bharati Gosani —
there Pandita Nimai will take sannyasa."[^39-2]

**3.** Vishvambhara fixed this with all His heart
and, setting out, noted that His breath was flowing through the right nostril.[^39-3]

**4.** Mahaprabhu went to the Ganga;
swimming across the Ganga, He went away, leaving Navadvipa.[^39-4]

**5.** Bowing to the Ganga, He leaves Navadvipa —
as if a thunderbolt struck everyone on the head.

**6.** As if the sun had hidden in the middle of the day,
as if the swans had left the lake — where have they gone?

**7.** As if life had suddenly left the body,
as if the bee had forsaken its love for the lotus.

**8.** Navadvipa was filled with the grief of separation:
a mountain of sorrow seemed to crush everyone.

**9.** Household and townsfolk, Shachi and Vishnupriya
fall senseless, throwing themselves on the ground.

**10.** Shachi-devi weeps, holding Vishnupriya on her lap —
and Vishnupriya lies as if dead:

**11.** the body is there, but life has left it.
Shachi and Vishnupriya weep, rolling on the ground.

**12.** Shachi-devi weeps and calls: "Nimai!" —
her heart blazes as if on fire:

**13.** "All around is empty, all is shrouded in darkness —
how shall I live in this dreadful house?

**14.** This house comes to swallow me;
the words of friends and kin are like poison.

**15.** No one will call me 'mother' any more;
even Yama will not take me — he too has forgotten me.

**16.** What sorrow has befallen me — my son has left me!
Making me a mother bereft of children, where has he gone?

**17.** Alas, alas, Nimai, you have become pitiless!
To what lands have you gone, my child? Who will bring you back?

**18.** My heart breaks, my son, remembering your sweetness:
never again will you call me 'mother', Gaurahari!

**19.** Leaving me helpless, where have you gone, my son?
It must have been in your mind to give your mother pain.

**20.** You studied, you heard [the scriptures], my son — and this is what you learned:
to make your mother helpless and wretched!

**21.** Where have you fled, abandoning Vishnupriya?
The love of all the devotees you counted as nothing!"

**22.** Vishnupriya weeps, her heart senseless;
now she rises, now she falls — her mind distracted.

**23.** She puts on no clothes, does not bind her hair,
she sobs aloud like a madwoman.

**24.** Pressing to her bosom the garland from the Lord's body, [she says]:
"Light a fire — I shall burn in it and die."

**25.** She cannot recount His qualities — she dies in the depths of her soul
and repeats only one thing: "What was in my fate [has come to pass]."

**26.** "Lord, all Your qualities are sweeter than nectar —
and now all of it has turned to fire."

**27.** Of their intimate joys she cannot even speak —
her heart burns, and she weeps in a piercing voice.

**28.** Around her the devotees are dying of anguish within —
what can be said? they cannot restrain themselves.

**29.** Gathering all their strength, they say to her softly, softly:
"How can we console you? Steady your life.

**30.** What you have seen, what you have heard all this time —
remember all that and steady your life.

**31.** Do you know whose He is — Bhagavan?
You have heard of all the former avataras.

**32.** His deeds are beyond the reach of the world and the Vedas;
by great fortune you bear the name of His kin.

**33.** Whatever He commanded each of us — so let us live:
carry out that command with a firm heart."

**34.** When the devotees spoke such words,
she listened and, aching at heart, held back her weeping.

**35.** Then Nityananda, gathering all the devotees,
takes counsel: "Where shall we go to see Him?"

**36.** Someone says: "Let us go round all the holy places —
somewhere we shall see Gorachand."

**37.** Someone says: "Let us go to Vrindavana, to Varanasi,
let us go to Nilachala, where the sannyasis live."

**38.** [Nityananda says:] "In Kanchananagara lives Bharati Gosani —
there Pandita Nimai will take sannyasa.

**39.** These words I once heard from the Lord's lips,
but I am not sure they are true.

**40.** If on a false report everyone rushes there, [it will be bad];
first I shall learn the truth and tell everyone.

**41.** Give me a few steadfast devotees to go with me —
I shall seize my Lord Gauranga and bring Him back."

**42.** Then all the devotees, considering,
gave Him several of the foremost as companions.

**43.** Sri Chandrashekhara Acharya, Pandita Damodara,
Vakreshvara and others set out in haste.

**44.** With them Nityananda departs,
having consoled the hearts of Shachi and Vishnupriya.

**45.** Meanwhile Gaurahari pressed swiftly on
with a beautiful gait, like a myriad maddened elephants.

**46.** Streams of prema pour from His eyes,
the golden youth's body thrills, its hairs on end.

**47.** Binding His hair with His upper cloth, the Lord
[goes] as if [Krishna] to fight the wrestlers of Mathura.[^39-5]

**48.** Overwhelmed by the bhava of separation from Radha, [He cries]:
"Where is Radha? Where has my Gokula gone?"[^39-6]

**49.** Now His pace slows,
now He slaps His arms, looking around on all sides.

**50.** Thus, absorbed in prema, goes along the road
my Lord Jagannatha, the guru of all.

**51.** The Lord Vishvambhara came to Kanchananagara,
where lived Keshava Bharati, the best of sannyasis.

**52.** With the highest devotion He bows [to him],
and the sannyasi, rising respectfully, utters: "Narayana!"[^39-7]

**53.** Both deem [the meeting] great good fortune and converse warmly;
Vishvambhara says: "Give Me sannyasa."

**54.** While the two were thus together,
Nityananda arrived with Chandrashekhara and the others.

**55.** Bowing to the sannyasi, they bow to the Lord;
the Lord, smiling, says: "It is good that you have come.

**56.** With your coming all is auspicious for Me:
I shall take sannyasa — and My life will be fulfilled."

**57.** Saying this, He again addresses Bharati,
bowing and pleading for sannyasa.

**58.** Bharati says: "Listen, listen, Vishvambhara:
my heart trembles at giving You sannyasa.

**59.** Such a beautiful body, such young years —
since birth You have known no sorrow or hardship.

**60.** You do not even have offspring —
it is not for me to give You sannyasa.

**61.** When You are past fifty and the passions subside,
then it will be reasonable to give You sannyasa."[^39-8]

**62.** Hearing this, the Lord says softly:
"What can I say in your presence?

**63.** Do not dissemble with Me, jewel among sannyasis:[^39-9]
who but you knows the truth of dharma and adharma?

**64.** In samsara this birth as a human being is rare,
and in it rare is devotion to Krishna, the highest dharma.

**65.** And rarer still the company of devotees;
and the human body perishes in a moment.

**66.** If, while I delay, this body passes away —
when then shall I have the company of Vaishnavas?

**67.** Do not dissemble with Me — give Me sannyasa:
by your grace may I become a servant of Krishna."

**68.** Saying this, [He stands]: His merciful eyes reddened,
brimming with tears, His face full of anguish.

**69.** Roaring, He surpasses a lion in might;
His whole body is bhava itself, marked with every auspicious sign.

**70.** "Hari, Hari!" He calls with the thunder of a cloud,
tears of prema flow ceaselessly from His eyes.

**71.** Standing thrice-bent, He calls: "The flute, the flute!";
now, crying "The circle of the rasa!", He bends His body.

**72.** He calls "Govardhana! Radha-kunda!" and laughs;
the sannyasi was astonished, trembling at heart.

**73.** Reflecting in his heart, the king of sannyasis speaks —
he understood within: "This is not a good deed for me.

**74.** He is the guru of the world, the Lord of the world —
and He will bow to me with joined palms, calling me guru."

**75.** Reasoning thus, the sannyasi answered:
"If You want to take sannyasa, go home.

**76.** Take leave of Your mother in person;
and Your virtuous wife — go to her.

**77.** Having taken leave of everyone in person
and made everyone understand, come to me."

**78.** But in his mind: "Having sent Gorachand away,
I shall leave my seat and go elsewhere."

**79.** Bhagavan, the Knower of hearts, understood this intention
and said with a smile: "I shall carry out your command."

**80.** Mahaprabhu set out for Navadvipa;
watching Him, the sannyasi thought to himself:

**81.** "He in whose pores hosts of universes dwell —
where, to what land, shall I flee from Him?

**82.** Deluded in mind, I look and do not see:
He is the life of all, the witness of all."

**83.** Thinking thus, the sannyasi called Gaurahari
and began to speak, entreating Him:

**84.** "I shall say one more word, listen, Vishvambhara:
I am very much afraid to give You sannyasa.

**85.** You are the guru of the world: who is Your guru?
Why do You needlessly put me in a false position?"

**86.** Hearing this, Vishvambhara Raya wept
and in longing clasped the sannyasi's feet:

**87.** "Why do you speak harsh words to one who bows before you?
Even dying I shall not let go of your feet.

**88.** All that you say to Me is to test My mind.
I have one request — hear My words.

**89.** One day at the end of the night I had a dream:
a brahmana told Me the sannyasa mantra.

**90.** See now — is it this or not?" —
and saying this, He speaks the mantra into Bharati's ear.

**91.** Thus He spoke the mantra into the sannyasi's ear:
on a plausible pretext the independent [Lord] Himself became the guru.[^39-10]

**91+1.** Hearing the mantra, the best of sannyasis was filled with prema:[^39-11]
trembling, thrilling, in tears, he says, "Radha-Krishna!"

**91+2.** Again and again he cries out: "Vrindavana! Yamuna!" —
he understood: "This is Krishna Himself, the son of Shachi.

**91+3.** Love for Him is the highest fortune;
dharma without love for Krishna is base."

**92.** Bharati Gosani understood everything:
"I shall give You sannyasa, listen, Nimai."

**93.** Hearing this, the Lord dances in joy
and cries "Hari, Hari!" in a deep voice like thunder.

**94.** On Gaura's golden body the hairs stood on end in rows —
the sweetness of His body seemed to spread out a market of nectar.

**95.** From His reddened eyes tears flow ceaselessly;
seeing this, all the people cry out in grief.

**95+1.** From Navadvipa came Gadadhara and Narahari[^39-12]
and joined [them], crying "Hari, Hari!"

**95+2.** Many times they prostrated themselves [before the Lord],
and Shachi's son, smiling, embraced them.

**95+3.** The Lord says: "It is good that you have come:
by Krishna's grace you have met [with Me].

**95+4.** You two are My companions from beginning to end;
seeing you, My heart rejoices."

**95+5.** Looking at Gaura's face, the two great souls weep;
both stand motionless to His right and left.

**96.** The people of Kanchananagara run to see;
whoever sees [Him] — his heart and eyes are refreshed.

**97.** Old people, the blind, women, men,
the learned and utter fools;

**98.** children run, and young women of noble families —
so secluded they had never seen even their own shadow.[^39-13]

**99.** One, with a pitcher on her hip, has stopped and stares;
one who cannot walk hurries along leaning on a stick.

**100.** The lame, the infirm, pregnant women —
seeing His holy body, they revile the sannyasi.

**100+1.** "Who gives sannyasa to such a boy?[^39-14]
It does not befit a sannyasi — people will laugh.

**100+2.** Hard is his heart, a pitiless man:
let us not keep him in the town!" — so they said.

**100+3.** All revile the sannyasi again and again,
yet looking at Gora's face, all are in boundless joy.

**101.** "Blessed, blessed!" people say, extolling His beauty:
"At last we have seen something so wondrous!

**102.** Blessed, blessed is the mother who bore [such] a son in her womb:
she is like Devaki, of whom we have heard of old.

**103.** What fortunate woman obtained such a husband?
In the three worlds there is none her equal in fortune.

**104.** Seeing His beauty, we cannot turn our eyes away —
how shall we bear His sannyasa?

**105.** How will His mother live?
Hearing of this, she will die at once.

**105+1.** We think He has neither mother nor father:[^39-15]
He is Himself unfailing bliss (Achyuta-ananda), the essence of all the Vedas.

**105+2.** Has He perhaps lost Radha in Vrindavana?
Searching for Her, He wanders weeping and weeping.

**105+3.** Because of that separation He is taking sannyasa:
we know for certain — this is the son of Nanda."

**106.** Thinking thus, all the people weep.
The Lord calls them and says: "Do not grieve.

**107.** Bless Me, mothers and fathers:
I long to lay My head at Krishna's feet.

**108.** She who has her own husband longs for him
and seeks means to bind his heart.

**109.** Beauty, youth, all this rasa and loveliness —
all is blessed when it serves one's own husband.

**110.** Consider this, all of you, in your hearts:
without a husband, all a young woman has is in vain.

**111.** Apart from Krishna's feet I have no other refuge:
giving My whole Self, I shall serve the Lord of My life."[^39-16]

**112.** Saying this, Mahaprabhu wept,
but in a moment He held everything back [within Himself].

[^39-1]: The OCR of the summary is badly garbled; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface (ch. 13: "consolation of the devotees by instruction in the truth; leaving home, the Lord goes to Kantakanagara, to Keshava Bharati; Nityananda Prabhu consoles Shachi and Vishnupriya; Nityananda's coming to Kantakanagara with Chandrashekhara Acharya, Damodara Pandita and other devotees; the Lord's request for the sannyasa mantra, Bharati's refusal and consent; the Lord speaks the sannyasa mantra into Bharati's ear; the grief of the villagers; the Lord consoles them and, as a devotee, asks for devotion to Krishna; the Lord's sannyasa name — Sri Krishna Chaitanya; after sannyasa, wandering in Radha-desha absorbed in prema"). The summary runs ahead: the end of the section is in the next portion. In the 1983 edition the disha is longer: "Why have You become like this, Gauranga, why? Why have You given up the dress of the best of dancers? Stand just a moment on the bank of the Ganga, Nimai, let me gaze at Your moon-face — and then go. If You will keep one word of mine, Nimai — no need of sannyasa, stay at home. Do not become a sannyasi, Nimai, do not become a vairagi, do not leave Your unfortunate mother. Your mother calls: stay, O Gauranga; do not go leaving Your mother, O Gauranga!"
[^39-2]: Kantakanagara is Katwa, on the west bank of the Ganga. Below (vv. 38, 51, 96) Lochana Dasa calls it Kanchananagara; the 1983 edition has that name here too.
[^39-3]: According to svara-shastra, setting out when the breath flows through the right nostril is a good omen.
[^39-4]: Cf. Murari 3.1.7: "Bhagavan crossed the divine river and went away."
[^39-5]: That is, like Krishna entering the arena against Kamsa's wrestlers in Mathura.
[^39-6]: Lochana Dasa here depicts the Lord in the mood of Krishna pining for Radha; according to the teaching of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (Krishnadasa Kaviraja, Bhaktisiddhanta), Gaura is Krishna in the bhava of Radha, tasting Her love for Krishna; so Bhaktisiddhanta also interprets a similar passage above (en/35, note 5).
[^39-7]: "Narayana!" is the customary greeting of sannyasis.
[^39-8]: Bharati speaks by the general rule of sannyasa, that with age the senses grow calm. Worldly passions have no bearing on the Lord, whose body and senses are spiritual; in the lila Bharati speaks to Him as to a young brahmana.
[^39-9]: Maya here means "dissembling, pretence": the Lord asks Bharati not to evade Him with plausible excuses. So also in v. 67.
[^39-10]: Cf. Murari 3.2.7–9: "Thus the one Lord of the worlds… on a plausible pretext gave initiation to His own guru." On the meaning of the sannyasa mantra see en/35, note 10. In the lila Keshava Bharati is the Lord's sannyasa-guru; the Lord, the guru of the world, Himself accepts him as guru in order to establish reverence for the guru.
[^39-11]: Vv. 91+1 — 91+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript (with one more couplet).
[^39-12]: Vv. 95+1 — 95+5 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Narahari Sarakara is Lochana Dasa's guru. The Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya 28) also speaks of Gadadhara at Katwa.
[^39-13]: Lit. "so beautiful that they do not recognise their own shadow" — that is, they live in seclusion and never go out in public.
[^39-14]: Vv. 100+1 — 100+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^39-15]: Vv. 105+1 — 105+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Vv. 96–107 — cf. Murari 3.1.10–18 and 3.2.2.
[^39-16]: The Lord speaks in the mood of a gopi: Krishna is the one husband of all souls (cf. en/38, note 7).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 17 (portion 40)

*The section "The Lord's Sannyasa" (conclusion): the Lord has His head shaved and receives the sannyasa mantra from Keshava Bharati; a voice from the sky names Him Sri Krishna Chaitanya; night kirtana with the guru; farewell to Bharati; three days absorbed in prema in Radha-desha; the Lord sends Chandrashekhara back to Navadvipa*

**113.** Again He bows to the best of sannyasis,
opens His heart to him and asks to be instructed in the rite.

**114.** The next day the Lord, taking the guru's leave,
smilingly performs the rites prescribed for sannyasa.

**115.** He performed all the prescribed rites,
His heart rejoicing: "I shall take sannyasa!"

**116.** Acharyaratna himself performs the worship of Krishna,[^40-1]
and all around the Vaishnavas cry, "Hari, Hari!"

**117.** With joined palms [the Lord] stands before the guru
and, bowing, asks for the sannyasa mantra.

**118.** The Lord had His head shaved — hear of it:
the heart of everyone who hears it aches.

**119.** The hearts of all the Vaishnavas trembled:
while He was being shaved, they covered their faces with cloth.

**120.** The hair cherished by Kamala, the most beautiful in the three worlds,
which with the garland flowed down to His shoulders, mighty as an elephant's;

**121.** which once, dressed in a chuda, enchanted the world,[^40-2] —
by meditating on it all the devotees live;

**122.** for its sake the cowherd wives gave up their modesty
and shattered, as with a thunderbolt, their fear for caste, family and good name;

**123.** singing its glory, Shiva, Brahma and Narada
count themselves blessed — it is all their wealth.

**124.** Such hair the Lord wishes to shave off, —
all the people weep and do not raise their faces.

**124+1.** Calling a barber, [the Lord] spoke to him humbly:[^40-3]
"Help Me in the service of Krishna.

**124+2.** Becoming a sannyasi, I shall belong to Krishna:
shave My head — it will be your good fortune."

**125.** The barber does not put his hand on His head:
his whole body trembles with fear.

**125+1.** "I have no such fortune, Lord, — I am utterly lost:[^40-4]
how can I put my hand on Your head?

**125+2.** Let me get leprosy and all my body rot away,
let me go to hell like Kamsa, — hear me, Gauranga, —

**125+3.** still I cannot put my hand on Your head:
humbly I tell You, hear me, Gaurahari."

**126.** The people of Kantakanagara, women and men,
weep aloud in piteous voices.

**127.** The barber says: "Lord, I pray at Your feet:
how could I dare to put my hand on Your head?

**128.** I have no strength to shave off
this beautiful curling hair that enchants the three worlds.

**129.** One look at it brings coolness to heart and eyes.
Do whatever You will, Lord, only do not shave it.

**130.** There is no one in the world like You:
You are the Lord of all the worlds — my heart has understood."

**131.** Hearing these words, the Lord is displeased;
seeing this, the barber grows afraid at heart

**132.** and pleads again, distressed within:
"How shall I put my hand on this head?

**133.** I fear to offend — my body shakes with fear:
having touched Your head, whose feet shall I touch afterwards?[^40-5]

**134.** Touching whose feet shall I ply my trade?
I am a lowly barber, of a wretched caste."

**135.** Hearing this, the compassionate Lord
says: "Ply your trade no more."

**135+1.** The Lord says:[^40-6] "Listen, Haridasa the barber:
shave Me — I shall take sannyasa.

**136.** By Krishna's grace you will pass your life happily,
and at the end your dwelling will be in My abode.

**136+1.** Having shaved Me, take all the instruments[^40-7]
and give them over to the waters of the Ganga."

**136+2.** Hearing this, Haridasa began to think:
"No good lot ever falls to me:

**136+3.** if I shave Him — ruin all the same,
and if I do not shave Him — I am undone.

**136+4.** I will do it out of love for Him — come what may:
here He is before my eyes — the Supreme Self, who rules dharma and adharma."

**137.** At the time of the shaving that barber received a blessing;
with a stricken heart Lochana Dasa sings of it.

*Raga purabi-sindhura*

**138.** Choosing an auspicious moment, the Lord had His head shaved
and takes sannyasa on the auspicious day of the sankranti.

**139.** At the hour when [the sun] leaves Makara and enters Kumbha,[^40-8]
the guru speaks the sannyasa mantra.

**140.** All around, the Vaishnavas perform sankirtana,
and the sannyasi speaks the mantra into Vishvambhara's ear.

**141.** Receiving the mantra, Vishvambhara thrilled all over:
the waves of love for Krishna swelled a hundredfold.

**142.** From His reddened eyes tears flow ceaselessly;
now He slaps His arms, now He lets out a roar.

**143.** "I have taken sannyasa!" He says, exulting,
and again and again laughs aloud in the bliss of prema.[^40-9]

**143+1.** [The people] of Kanchananagara, seeing that beauty,[^40-10]
knew for certain: this is He who delights in the rasa dance.

**143+2.** Gazing at His face, the devotees dance in bliss;
the Lord Himself dances, Nityananda dances.

**143+3.** Cymbals ring, mridangas sound, the kirtana roars,
and all the people around cry, "Haribol!"

**143+4.** The crest-jewel of dancers, with His skilful companions,
sings the glories of Radha and Krishna, overwhelmed with prema.

**144.** At that very time Bharati Gosani says:
"What will Your name be? Listen, Nimai."

**145.** All the Vaishnavas who were there,
together with the best of sannyasis, began to deliberate;

**146.** each speaks according to his understanding, whatever is in his mind, —
and then an auspicious voice arose in the sky.

**147.** Hearing it, everyone was amazed:
"Give Him the name 'Sri Krishna Chaitanya.'"

**148.** Mahamaya, the goddess Bhagavati, in the form of sleep,
has enveloped all people — their minds are clouded;

**149.** all that they do is [as] in a dream.
The Lord Himself awakens everyone;

**150.** being Himself Krishna, He reveals Krishna to all —
therefore He is called "Sri Krishna Chaitanya."[^40-11]

**151.** When all heard these words from the mouth of heaven,
the people joyfully raised the cry of Hari's name.

**151+1.** With a joyful heart the Lord cries, "Haribol!":[^40-12]
"From today My name is Sri Krishna Chaitanya."

**151+2.** Bowing many times at the guru's feet,
Vishvambhara circumambulated him and set out.

**151+3.** Seeing Him leaving, the king of sannyasis
calls: "Here, take the staff, do not delay!"

**151+4.** Hearing the guru's word, [the Lord] came back
and, smiling gently, received the staff from the guru.

**151+5.** The Lord reverently accepted the guru's staff
and bowed to him many times with fervent devotion.

**151+6.** Then Mahaprabhu cries, "Haribol!" —
and the waves of great prema surge up.

**152.** By the guru's order the Lord stayed there that day
and spent it happily in service to the guru.

**153.** At night the Vaishnavas together perform sankirtana;
together with the guru [the Lord] dances enchantingly.

**154.** Keshava Bharati dances in the joy of prema's bliss,
the Lord dances, and all the people cry, "Hari!"

**155.** Both, filled with the bliss of prema, forgot themselves;
both regard the happiness of Brahman as paltry.[^40-13]

**155+1.** Thus after a while, when the dancing ended,[^40-14]
the sannyasi sat down and spoke, and Vishvambhara listened:

**155+2.** "Who took the staff from my hand?
Touching the tip of the staff, I want to dance again!"

**155+3.** Saying this, he dances, beside himself —
dances wondrously, brimming with the bliss of prema.

**155+4.** In bliss all the Vaishnavas dance joyfully,
and all the people around cry, "Hari, Hari!"

**156.** Thus in joy and delight the night passes;
rising at dawn, the Lord asks leave to go.

**157.** Circumambulating the guru, He bows:
"I shall go to Nilachala, if I have your sanction."

**158.** The Lord asks permission at the guru's feet, —
and Keshava Bharati's heart throbs.

**159.** His eyes brim with tears of compassion;
at parting he embraces Gorachand:

**160.** "You are the independent Lord, [abiding] in Your own bliss;
out of compassion You walk on foot among people.

**161.** To teach [people] devotion to the guru You perform the prescribed rites,
to establish the dharma of sankirtana.

**162.** To deliver all people You have shown mercy,
and to put me in an awkward place You have taken this sannyasa.[^40-15]

**163.** That I too may be delivered, Vishvambhara —
this is my plea; keep it in Your heart."

**163+1.** [Bharati] gave leave: "Go to Nilachala, the king of mountains,"[^40-16]
and Gaurachandra, out of modesty, said nothing more.

**164.** Touching his feet, the Lord set out;
on the road the bliss of prema grew beyond measure.

**165.** In the exultation of prema He calls, "Krishna, Krishna!" —
now He weeps, now He laughs aloud.

**166.** Streams of tears run down His chest —
like the stream of the celestial river down the peak of Sumeru.

**167.** His abundant thrill surpasses the filaments of the kadamba:
His whole body bristles from head to foot.

**168.** He moves along playfully like an intoxicated elephant;
in boundless prema now He sings "Krishna!",

**169.** now He falls to the ground and lies stunned,
now He springs up crying, "Haribol!"

**170.** Now the bhava of a gopi is in Him, now the bhava of a servant;[^40-17]
now He walks slowly, now He runs swiftly.

**171.** Thus in bliss He knows neither day nor night.
In Radha-desha He did not hear even a trace of Krishna's name,[^40-18]

**172.** and, not hearing Krishna's name, He was filled with grief
and resolved to enter the water.[^40-19]

**173.** Seeing this, all the devotees lament:
"Gauranga is going to Goloka! Alas, what will become of us!"

**174.** Then Nityananda Prabhu says with a hero's boldness:
"By my own power I shall keep Chaitanya here."

**175.** There some boys were grazing cows —
Nityananda Prabhu entered their hearts.

**176.** When [the Lord], resolved, went near the water,
one boy suddenly called out, "Hari!"

**177.** Hearing this, Gaurahari turned back
and, holding the boy's hand, urges him: "Say it, say it [again]!

**178.** May the Lord Bhagavan be merciful to you:
you have blessed Me by letting Me hear the name of Hari."

**179.** The Lord floats in the bliss of prema, His heart rejoicing;
going on a little further, the Lord took alms.

**180.** Thus in bliss He knew neither day nor night —
only after three days did He take food and water.

**181.** Thus days and nights passed in the bliss of prema.
[Then the Lord] dismissed Chandrashekhara Acharya:

**181+1.** "All the people of Navadvipa[^40-20]
weep for Me in distress and keep calling Me.

**181+2.** They do not know for certain that I have taken sannyasa:
tell them all this account of Me."

**182.** The Lord said: "We shall see each other again;
soon we shall meet — do not be downcast."

**183.** Saying this, the Lord went on quickly,
and Sri Chandrashekhara goes away weeping:

**183+1.** "I shall die, I shall die, Lord, not seeing You,[^40-21]
I shall die, fallen into Navadvipa's ocean of grief!"

**184.** Meanwhile the people of Navadvipa wait, their eyes fixed on the road:
what news will Sri Chandrashekhara bring when he comes?

**185.** Says Lochana: this cannot be told in words.
Sri Chandrashekhara Acharya reaches Navadvipa.

[^40-1]: Acharyaratna is Chandrashekhara Acharya, who came to Kantakanagara with Nityananda (en/39, v. 43). Cf. Murari 3.2.6.
[^40-2]: "Once" — in Krishna's lila in Vraja; a chuda is a topknot adorned with a peacock feather. Lochana Dasa speaks of Gaura's hair as the hair of Krishna Himself.
[^40-3]: Vv. 124+1 — 124+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^40-4]: Vv. 125+1 — 125+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have them. The second line of v. 125+2 is unclear in the 1983 edition; we understand it as "let me go to hell like Kamsa".
[^40-5]: A barber's trade is to shave and pare nails, touching people's feet; having touched the Lord's head, he can no longer touch the feet of others.
[^40-6]: V. 135+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have it. The barber is called Haridasa here — not to be confused with Haridasa Thakura.
[^40-7]: Vv. 136+1 — 136+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript (with the lines of v. 136+3 in reverse order).
[^40-8]: That is, at the sun's passage from Capricorn into Aquarius — at the end of the solar month of Magha (January–February). Cf. Murari 3.2.10.
[^40-9]: Cf. Murari 3.2.11. In the 1983 edition a new chapter, "Acceptance of Sannyasa", begins at v. 138; in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition the section continues.
[^40-10]: Vv. 143+1 — 143+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript (with one more couplet: Gadadhara and Narahari dance beside the Lord, and all the Vaishnavas dance around Gora).
[^40-11]: Chaitanya means "consciousness", "awakening". Mahamaya keeps in oblivion the living beings who have turned away from Krishna; the Lord awakens them to Krishna consciousness. In the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Madhya 28) Keshava Bharati himself gives the Lord this name, with the same explanation; in Lochana Dasa it is proclaimed by a voice from the sky.
[^40-12]: Vv. 151+1 — 151+6 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition vv. 151+1 — 151+5 stand in the main text and v. 151+6 is in a footnote, as part of an additional reading of one manuscript. Cf. Murari 3.2.12: the guru himself puts the staff, together with the cloth, into the Lord's hand.
[^40-13]: Cf. Murari 3.2.13–14.
[^40-14]: Vv. 155+1 — 155+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they stand in the main text. Cf. Murari 3.2.15–18: Bharati relates that someone snatched the staff from his hands and told him to dance.
[^40-15]: In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition v. 162 is misnumbered 161. Bharati speaks in humility: the Lord, the guru of the world, has placed him in the position of His guru (cf. en/39, v. 85).
[^40-16]: V. 163+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition it stands in the main text. Nilachala ("the Blue Mountain") is Puri, the abode of Jagannatha.
[^40-17]: Cf. Murari 3.3.17.
[^40-18]: Radha-desha (Rarh) is the region west of the Ganga. Vv. 164–180 — cf. Murari 3.3.1–10, 16–18; Chaitanya-bhagavata, Antya 1.
[^40-19]: As in Murari (3.3.6), the Lord speaks and acts in the mood of a devotee for whom life without the name of Hari is unbearable; this is a manifestation of prema, not the despair of a conditioned soul. The Lord's body is spiritual, and He, the independent one, is free to manifest and withdraw it.
[^40-20]: Vv. 181+1 — 181+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Vv. 181–185 — cf. Murari 3.3.11–14, 19.
[^40-21]: V. 183+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition it is in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript (there: "burnt in the fire of Navadvipa's grief").

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 18 (portion 41)

*The section "The Lord's Arrival at Shantipura": the lament of Shachi and Vishnupriya on Chandrashekhara's return; the devotees of Navadvipa chant the Name, and the Lord, bound by it, sends Nityananda for them; Nityananda in Navadvipa; Shachi and all the people of Nadia go to Advaita's house; the Lord comes to Shantipura; the meal and kirtana*

## The Lord's Arrival at Shantipura

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* When Chandrashekhara Acharya returned to Nadia, at the sight of him the fire of Shachi's and Vishnupriya's grief blazed up twice as fierce; lamenting bitterly, they began to question the Acharya about Sriman Mahaprabhu. Meanwhile Bhagavan Gaurahari, the Knower of hearts, [seeing] the anguish of the people of Nadia and wishing to show Himself to them, resolved to come to Shantipura and sent them word of it through Sri Nityananda Prabhu. As soon as Nityananda Prabhu arrived in Nadia, at the sight of Him life returned to the bodies of the people of Nadia, who were wasting away in separation. When Shachidevi began to weep, Nityananda Prabhu consoled her and told her of the Lord's coming to Shantipura. Hearing this news, all the people of Nadia in great joy [came] to the house of Sri Advaita [Acharya], to the feet of Sriman Mahaprabhu, and the Lord received them fittingly. Thus that night passed in great bliss.[^41-1]

*Raga karunashri; disha: "Oh, alas, alas!"*

**1.** As Acharya Shekhara entered Navadvipa,
tears flowed ceaselessly from his eyes.

**2.** All the people of Navadvipa, seeing him,
burn within, their hearts pounding.

**3.** All the Vaishnavas came and gathered there;
unable to hold back their tears, with stricken faces,

**4.** they cannot ask anything — no word comes from their lips.
Hearing [of his return], Shachidevi runs with her hair dishevelled.

**5.** Frantic with grief, she calls, "Acharya!" —
and, not seeing Gauranga, she is thrown into turmoil:

**6.** "Where have you left my Nimai and come back?
How did He shave His head, in what land?

**7.** What wretched sannyasi with a cruel heart
felt no pity when giving the mantra to Vishvambhara![^41-2]

**8.** Seeing such loveliness of beautiful hair, —
what wretched barber with a pitiless heart!

**9.** What sinner put a razor to such hair?
How did he survive, that pitiless, cruel one?

**10.** In whose house did my Nimai take alms?
How does my child look with His head shaven?

**11.** I shall see your face no more, my son:
my whole world has become dark.

**12.** No more shall I cook rice to serve you,
no more shall I touch your beautiful body,

**13.** no more shall I kiss your lovely face.
Who will know when you are hungry?"

**14.** While Shachidevi was lamenting thus,
some people went to console Vishnupriya.

**15.** At Vishnupriya's weeping the earth splits;
beasts, birds, creepers, trees — even the stones weep.[^41-3]

**15+1.** "Alas, alas, Lord of my life, You have left Nadia,[^41-4]
cruelly forsaking helpless Vishnupriya!

**15+2.** The joy of kirtana with Srivasa and the other devotees,
Your dancing — I shall not see them to my eyes' fill again.

**15+3.** Not hearing the words from Your beautiful lips, faltering with prema,
how will this unfortunate one live?

**15+4.** In what land are You, and how, Lord of my life?
I remember and remember — and my life is worn away.

**15+5.** Alas, hard life, why do you not depart?
Kindle a fire — I shall die at once!

**15+6.** In my anguish each day has become as millions of yugas:
I do not see, Lord of my life, Your moon-like face.

**15+7.** A saint causes no anguish to any living being, —
yet in grief for You Mother Shachi is giving up her life.

**15+8.** Unfortunate, I did not know how to serve You with devotion —
surely for that offence I have been left forlorn.

**15+9.** No more shall I find solace, Lord,
sitting at Your feet and gazing and gazing at Your beauty,

**15+10.** raising betel with camphor to Your lips,
touching with my fingers the pearl-like row of Your teeth.

**15+11.** You would look tenderly from the corners of Your reddish eyes
and, smiling, speak sweet, sweet words.

**15+12.** The reddish lips, redder still with betel,
the gleam of the teeth — all rise up in my heart,

**15+13.** and on the face a smile steeped in nectar:
only by the hope of it do my ears and eyes live.

**15+14.** All Your virtues, Lord, are sweeter than nectar, —
but now, when I remember them, they have turned to fire.

**15+15.** Delightful pastimes and their sweetness, the happy bed, —
remembering all this, Vishnupriya is giving up her life.

**16.** Alas, alas, what fate has befallen me!
Without Gaura all is darkness for me.

**17.** I shall see that smile, that loveliness of body no more,
I shall not hear the clever words, the essence of nectar.

**18.** Leaving me forlorn, where have You gone?
I remember Your virtues and pour out my grief.

**19.** What fortunate woman who had seen You
has not reproached me with tears?

**20.** From the embrace of what unfortunate woman have You gone?
Why did she not die, the unfortunate one whose vow is broken?[^41-5]

**21.** I worshipped Your face with loving eyes —
how shall I hold my heart without seeing You?

**22.** All the noble women who love You are dying in separation,[^41-6]
and I, unfortunate, have kept this body so long.

**23.** I die, I die — where has beautiful Gauranga gone?
I am a woman, forlorn and weak by nature.

**24.** To what land shall I go, where shall I find You?
No one will let me go — I shall die right here.

**25.** Leaving Your mother forlorn, to what land have You gone?
How will she live, burning with longing for You?

**26.** Life will not leave my sinful body!" —
rolling on the ground, the goddess cries, "Alas, alas!"

**27.** Breath hot with the fire of separation flows ceaselessly;
her lips dry up, her body trembles.

**28.** Lying in the dust, she tends neither hair nor clothes;
now her body grows faint, now it seems to swell.

**29.** Now she swoons, meditating on His reddish feet,
now with great effort she comes to herself.

**30.** Now she calls with a wail, "Lord, Lord!" —
and at Vishnupriya's weeping everyone weeps.

**31.** All who went to console her —
seeing Vishnupriya, their hearts began to burn.

**31+1.** They call into her ear, "Gauranga, Gauranga!"[^41-7] —
and after a while Vishnupriya came to herself.

**32.** Everyone says: "Listen, Vishnupriya,
what consolation can we give you? Steady your heart.

**32+1.** Your Lord told you:[^41-8]
'Wherever I go, I am always near you.'

**33.** The doings of your Lord are not unknown to you:
understand this and console your own heart."

**34.** Having consoled her, all the devotees gathered together
and took counsel about Gorachand:

**35.** "He has taken sannyasa, causing grief to us all,
and pitilessly left us here.

**36.** How shall we live without Him?
Pitilessly Gora has left us all.

**37.** But His great name is more merciful than He Himself:
through the name we shall attain Him — this is the chief thing.[^41-9]

**38.** There is His word, spoken to us before:
'Whoever takes My name will attain Me.'"

**39.** Thinking thus, all sat down to chant the name —
Shachi, Vishnupriya and all the others,

**40.** children and the old, young men and women —
sat down to chant the name, taking Gauranga as their refuge.

**41.** With the noose of the name they bound Gauranga, the maddened lion:
Mahaprabhu stopped, His going was broken off.[^41-10]

**42.** Leaning on Nityananda, He stood
and wept, His eyes reddened.

**43.** "Go to Navadvipa today, Nityananda,
so that I may see everyone at Shantipura."

**44.** Hearing this, Nityananda rejoiced at heart,
and [the Lord] gave His word: "I will show Myself to all."

**45.** With a grieving heart Lochana Dasa says:
then the Lord Gorachand set out.

**46.** Sri Nityananda Prabhu goes along with Him;[^41-11]
the Lord, smiling, sent Him off:

**47.** "Go to Navadvipa, hear My word:
to all My dear ones in the town of Nadia

**48.** give My greeting, 'Narayana':[^41-12]
I shall stay at the house of Advaita Acharya.

**49.** Bring everyone and come there —
we shall all meet together at the Acharya's house."

**50.** Saying this, Mahaprabhu went on quickly,
and Nityananda goes to the town of Nadia.

**51.** The people of the town of Nadia are dead while living:
cut them, chop them — there is no blood or flesh in them;

**52.** no food in their stomachs, their bodies totter —
without Gorachand all is darkness to them.

**53.** Suddenly — Nityananda in the town of Nadia!
Strength came into their bodies, and all ran to Him quickly.

**54.** They cannot walk, they totter on the road,
they cannot see the way for their tears.

**55.** All the Vaishnavas weep, falling at His feet;
they cannot ask anything — they only gaze at His face.

**56.** Shachi, utterly frantic, runs with her face turned to the sky;
Shachi's grief filled earth and sky.

**57.** With a wail Shachi calls: "O Avadhuta,
where have you left my Nimai, my golden son, and come back?"

**58.** Saying this, Shachi weeps and beats her breast;
she totters, not looking to the road.

**59.** Seeing Shachi, Thakura rose to greet her.[^41-13]
Shachi says: "How far off is my son?"

**60.** Nityananda says: "Do not grieve at heart:
He has sent me to bring you all.

**61.** The Lord will stay at Advaita Acharya's house.
Do not grieve — you will see Him soon.

**62.** Come, everyone, to see the Lord!" —
and at that very moment everyone set out:

**63.** from children to the old, young women, the mute, the sober,
the ignorant and the ascetics — everyone set out.

**64.** Shachi runs ahead — strength has come into her body;
joyfully all the Vaishnavas go along.

**65.** Arriving at Advaita Acharya's house
and not seeing the Lord, they lost heart.[^41-14]

**66.** Nityananda says to Advaita Acharya:
"The Lord has resolved to stay at Your abode.

**67.** He sent me to bring all these;
what more is in His heart I do not know."

**68.** Saying this, the two embrace;
hearing of Gauranga's sannyasa, Advaita is distraught:

**69.** "Unfortunate that I am, I was not with Him.
When shall I see His moon-like face again?"

**70.** Frantic, Shachi keeps asking again and again,
and everyone says: "The Lord will come any moment."

**71.** Eagerness grew in everyone's heart —
and at that very time Mahaprabhu came.[^41-15]

**72.** The radiance of His body was millions of times greater than before,
and on it a bright, long mark of sandal.

**73.** On the golden body the reddish cloth shines,
surpassing in colour the morning sun.

**74.** Staff in hand, the Lord comes with a lion's gait;
seeing Him, all the people fell at His feet.

**75.** Seeing the radiance of His body, their hearts were soothed,
and all forgot hundreds of thousands of sorrows.

**75+1.** Hearts filled with joy — no sorrow, no grief;[^41-16]
all gaze fixedly at Vishvambhara's face.

**75+2.** As a man who has lost his life regains life,
as a rich man who has lost his wealth regains his wealth,

**75+3.** as faithful wives who have lost their husbands
are happy to see them again,

**75+4.** as a fish that struggles out of water
rejoices when suddenly it finds water —

**75+5.** so all the people, seeing Gauranga,
thrill in every limb, their hearts rejoicing.

**76.** Shachi's heart filled with prema — no sorrow, no grief;
she gazes fixedly at Vishvambhara's face.

**76+1.** "Come, come, my son, son of a mother bereft of her children![^41-17]
Leaving me forlorn, where did you go?

**76+2.** I shall take you home and keep you safe,
removing all your sannyasi dress."

**76+3.** Seeing His mother weep, the Lord of the world,
Vishvambhara, fell before her like a staff

**76+4.** and says to His mother: "Weep no more:
your weeping pains My heart."

**76+5.** Saying this, Bhagavan dispelled her grief,
and Shachi checked her sorrow.

**77.** All the grief there had been left their hearts:
His face, as they gazed at it, sprinkled them with nectar.

**78.** Advaita Acharya Gosani, with a joyful heart,
seated the Lord Gora Raya on a splendid seat.

**79.** Washing His feet and wiping them with cloth,
all His own people drank the water from His feet.

**80.** Cries of "Jaya, jaya!" and "Hari, Hari!" resound —
waves of bliss in the hearts of all the Vaishnavas.

**81.** Seeing His effulgence, Haridasa rejoiced,
and Murari, Mukunda Datta and Srinivasa.

**82.** Falling on the ground, they prostrate like staffs;
seeing His face, their eyes brim with tears.

**83.** Their voices are choked with joy, their bodies thrill:
life suddenly returned to dead bodies.

**84.** Thus Gora Raya, seeing His own,
looks on them with a merciful glance — compassion swells in His heart.

**85.** Some the Lord touches with His own hand,
some He greets with a smile, some He clasps to His breast —

**86.** the Lord fulfils each one's desire,
and prema grew abundantly in everyone's heart.

**87.** Everyone was delighted, grief went far away;
joyfully the people raise the auspicious cry of "Hari!"

**88.** At the abode of the wise devotee Advaita Acharya Gosani
the Lord took His meal.[^41-18]

**88+1.** Mother Shachi, the mother of the world, cooked;[^41-19]
Sita-devi and Narayani floated in bliss.

**88+2.** Advaita serves [the Lord] with great care:
all the dishes, most delicious, He placed on His leaf.

**88+3.** The Lord, the King of the gods, eats,
and, seeing this, all the devotees rejoice at heart.

**89.** And all the others, each as befitted him,
ate in joy and merriment.

**90.** No one remembers that the Lord has taken sannyasa:
joyfully they spend days and nights in sankirtana.

**91.** Absorbed in sankirtana, the Lord sings His own glories;[^41-20]
with a joyful heart He dances Himself and makes others dance.

**91+1.** Nityananda dances, and Haridasa dances,[^41-21]
Murari, Mukunda and Srinivasa dance;

**91+2.** beside Him Gadadhara and Narahari dance,
Vasudeva Ghosha and Gadadhara Dasa dance.

**91+3.** All the devotees dance around my Gauranga —
I cannot count them, naming them one by one.

**91+4.** Countless are Gauranga's companions — who can name them?
All dance around the Lord Vishvambhara.

**92.** All the devotees dance in the delight of prema's rasa;
Advaita Acharya dances with His son.[^41-22]

**93.** In everyone's heart prema grew boundlessly —
tears, trembling, thrill and the other sattvika transformations.

**94.** In everyone's heart arose joy and exultation.
Hearing this, Lochana Dasa is happy.

[^41-1]: The OCR of the summary is garbled in places; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface (ch. 14: "Chandrashekhara Acharya's coming from Kantakanagara to Nadia; the grief and lament of Mother Shachi and Vishnupriya; Nityananda Prabhu announces Mahaprabhu's coming to Shantipura; Nityananda Prabhu's conversation with Mother Shachi; the people of Nadia come to Advaita's house to see the Lord; Mahaprabhu speaks fittingly with everyone").
[^41-2]: Words of a mother's grief; cf. en/39, vv. 100+1–100+3. In the lila Keshava Bharati is the Lord's revered sannyasa-guru (en/39, note 10).
[^41-3]: Vishnupriya is the Lord's eternal consort, His Bhu-shakti (en/21, note 9); her separation from the Lord is not worldly grief (cf. en/38, note 5).
[^41-4]: Vv. 15+1 — 15+15 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have them.
[^41-5]: Lit. "whose vow is broken": Vishnupriya apparently reproaches herself for having failed in the vow of a faithful wife — to keep her husband and serve him.
[^41-6]: Lit. "all Your worthy women" — the women of Nadia devoted to the Lord; cf. en/09, note 9.
[^41-7]: V. 31+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have it.
[^41-8]: V. 32+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have it. Cf. en/38 (the Lord consoles Vishnupriya before His sannyasa).
[^41-9]: The Lord's name is not different from Him, yet it is more merciful than He, for it is open to all — a common teaching of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas.
[^41-10]: The independent Lord submits to the love of His devotees and to their chanting of His name.
[^41-11]: In the 1983 edition a new chapter, "Pastimes at Shantipura", begins at v. 46. Vv. 46–64 — cf. Murari 3.4.3–10.
[^41-12]: The customary greeting of sannyasis (en/39, note 7); cf. Murari 3.3.13. The 1983 edition reads "Namo narayanaya".
[^41-13]: Thakura here is Nityananda.
[^41-14]: Lit. "their backs broke". In Bhaktisiddhanta's edition the number of v. 65 is printed as "1"; the numbers of vv. 60 and 85 are missing, and vv. 62 and 92 are printed as 61 and 91 — restored.
[^41-15]: Vv. 71–90 — cf. Murari 3.4.12–23; Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 3.
[^41-16]: Vv. 75+1 — 75+5 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition vv. 75+2 — 75+5 are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. V. 75+1 is close to v. 76.
[^41-17]: Vv. 76+1 — 76+5 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have them.
[^41-18]: Bhiksha is a sannyasi's meal, taken in a householder's home.
[^41-19]: Vv. 88+1 — 88+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Sita-devi is Advaita's wife; Narayani is probably Srivasa's niece, later the mother of Vrindavana Dasa (possibly also an epithet of Sita). The Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 3) also says that Shachi cooked at Advaita's house.
[^41-20]: The Lord is Himself Krishna; singing of Krishna, He sings of Himself.
[^41-21]: Vv. 91+1 — 91+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; in the 1903 edition they are in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^41-22]: The 1983 edition has another redaction of this couplet: "Shachi watches happily together with Sita and Narayani; Advaita Acharya dances merrily with His son."

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 19 (portion 42)

*The section "The Lord's Journey to Nilachala; the Broken Staff" (beginning): at Shantipura the Lord takes leave of the devotees and tells them to chant the name of Hari; Haridasa's plea; the devotees' sorrowful speeches; the Lord promises to live in Nilachala and consoles Shachi; Advaita laments that He feels no prema, and the Lord unties the knot of His cloth*

## The Lord's Journey to Nilachala; the Broken Staff

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* [Then] Bhagavan, having instructed the devotees to do good to all living beings by ceaseless chanting of the name of Hari, prepared to go to Nilachala; Thakura Haridasa, falling at His feet, poured out a humble and sorrowful plea. When the other devotees, telling of the grief of Shachi and Vishnupriya, began to follow the Lord, [the Lord] consoled them and Shachidevi with sweet words and, absorbed in prema, reciting the verse "Rama Raghava, Rama Raghava, Rama Raghava, protect me" and others, set out for Nilachala. Then, [when, having given His staff to Nityananda,] He was walking absorbed in prema in an excess of bliss, Nityananda Prabhu broke His staff, and Gaurahari showed anger towards Him.[^42-1]

*Raga bhatiyari; disha: "Brothers, O brothers, sing the glory and the virtues of Gora Gosani! (murchha) O brothers, my dear brothers, give up worldly desires: as long as you live in the world, do not leave the feet of Mahaprabhu!"*

**1.** Thus the auspicious night turned to a bright morning;
having done His morning duties, the Lord sat on His seat.

**2.** Staff in hand — like the sovereign of all kingdoms;
the reddish cloth glitters on His body.

**3.** To all His own people sitting near Him
the Lord, smiling, speaks thus:

**4.** "Srinivasa and all you devotees,
go, all of you, to your homes.

**5.** I shall go to Nilachala to see Jagannatha,
if the Lord, with a gracious face, shows Me mercy.[^42-2]

**6.** And you stay here and carry out My order:
ceaselessly, day and night, perform kirtana.

**7.** Establish the name of Hari and service to devotees,
so that, following this dharma, all people may be delivered.

**8.** Let everyone's heart be free from envy;
all of you, honour one another's hearts."

**9.** Saying this, the Lord rose quickly
and, opening His arms, embraces everyone.

**10.** Both His eyes brim with tears of prema,
His throat is tight with tenderness, His voice falters.

**11.** At that very time the venerable Haridasa,
holding a straw between his teeth,[^42-3] falls at His lotus feet.

**12.** He weeps with a loud wail, in a piteous voice;
the heart of everyone who hears it breaks.

**13.** The Lord was pained, His eyes filled with tears;
with an aching heart He speaks:

**14.** "When will such good fortune be Mine —
to fall and weep at Jagannatha's feet?

**15.** Reaching His lotus feet, I shall tell Him of My distress
and, seeing His face, make My eyes fruitful."[^42-4]

**16.** As He said this, all the devotees around
fell on the ground and wept.

**17.** Shachi lost her senses and cannot even weep —
she only wants to clasp her son by the neck.

**18.** Some weep with dishevelled hair, clasping His feet;
then, with great effort composing themselves,

**19.** Srinivasa, Haridasa, Murari and Mukunda
venture to say to the Lord:

**20.** "You are the independent Lord, and we are all dependent,
wretched, wicked, sinful, and devoid of devotion besides.

**21.** What can we say, Lord? You have taken sannyasa
and now You are leaving, forsaking all Your servants.

**22.** How will You walk the road all alone?
From whom will You ask food in hunger and thirst?

**23.** You are Shachi's darling, of matchless character;
Vishnupriya served Your feet.

**24.** By Your glance, nectar to the devotees' eyes,
the tree of prema grew in this body before our eyes.

**25.** Great was our hope for the fruit of prema —
and You, taking sannyasa, have emptied that hope.

**26.** Life will not leave our sinful bodies —
and so, having seen You off, we shall go home!

**27.** Are we lowly ones now to go away?
That is not Your way: You are the purifier of the fallen.

**28.** The Creator fashioned Your body from the clay of compassion,
adding the many treasures of delightful pastimes;

**29.** into pure supreme prema he breathed life —
and You manifested a form wondrous in the three worlds.[^42-5]

**30.** There is none to compare with You in the three worlds, —
yet at Your cruel words the world is in anguish.

**31.** It does not befit You to act thus, Lord:
having planted the tree Yourself, why do You cut it at the root?

**32.** Whoever would go — take them with You,
or else we shall all die, burning in fire.

**33.** Look at Your mother Shachi, forlorn:
we cannot bear her plaintive weeping.

**34.** At Vishnupriya's weeping the earth splits;
Navadvipa has become empty — town and market alike.

**35.** The houses of all the Vaishnavas seem empty,
and each other's houses seem yojanas apart.

**36.** Where You sat, Lord, and spoke of Yourself —
if I see that place I shall die; I shall go there no more.

**37.** I shall hear no more intimate, delightful talks,
I shall not see the rapture of dancing, the spreading of prema.

**38.** At the time of dancing You will embrace us no more;
I shall not see tears of prema in Your reddish eyes.

**39.** I shall hear no more the nectar of Your roaring:
who has shut the doors of my ears and eyes?

**40.** How shall I live without seeing Your moon-like face?
Who has blinded me while I still have eyes?

**41.** Do not send us away, Lord — we shall go with You:
Your cruel words burn our whole body.

**42.** As a hunter, with the sound of a bell,
lures a doe near and then seizes and kills her, —

**43.** such, it seems, is Your love too — now we understand.
Why, having enticed us, do You then kill us?

**44.** In separation from You all the devotees will die —
how then will You bear the name 'Affectionate to devotees'?

**45.** How will You take leave of Shachi?
Who will tell her this?

**46.** Vishnupriya will die on merely hearing of it:
settle this matter Yourself."

**47.** When the devotees had spoken such words,
the Lord, compassionate at heart, began to speak:

**48.** "Hear My word in full, all you devotees:
never shall I be cruel to you.

**49.** I shall surely dwell in Nilachala;
you will always come there and see Me.[^42-6]

**50.** Prema will grow boundlessly, more than before;
the world will float in the sankirtana of Hari's name.

**51.** In no one's heart shall I leave sorrow and grief:
I shall immerse all people in the ocean of sankirtana.

**52.** Be it Vishnupriya or My mother Shachi —
whoever worships Krishna, in that one's embrace I abide."

**53.** Hearing this, all fell at His feet:
"Swear to it, Lord — to what You have said!"

**54.** "Truly, truly, truly," the Lord repeats again and again,
"truly I shall dwell in Nilachala."

**55.** Shachidevi cannot stand steadily on her feet;
she rose, holding on to the hands of two people:

**56.** "Where will you go, having become pitiless?
Not seeing you, my son, I shall die.

**57.** Everyone else will see your face many times —
but I, unfortunate, shall see it no more.

**58.** You yourself, child, have consoled everyone —
but who, my son, will console me?

**59.** I have no one else in this world,
and Vishnupriya is like a spear lodged in my breast."

**60.** Smiling, with a compassionate heart, the Lord says:
"You are dying of vain grief, forgetting what you knew before.[^42-7]

**61.** Go home, keep no grief in your heart,
live with everyone, free from envy."

**62.** Prostrating at His mother's feet,
the Lord consoled her with well-ordered words.

**63.** Having consoled His mother, the Lord cries "Haribol!"
and goes off quickly — a loud wailing arises.

**64.** Advaita Acharya goes along with the Lord;
after going two dandas,[^42-8] the Lord looks back.

**65.** Mahaprabhu stopped, for the Acharya had fallen behind;
the Acharya came up, holding His waist,

**66.** His face drawn, beads of sweat upon it,
and with an aching heart He asks the Lord:

**67.** "You are going to a foreign land — that is my sorrow;
but besides it another thing burns my breast.

**68.** I make known to You what is in my heart —
surely, Lord, You will answer me.

**69.** All Your own people, in separation from You,
weep in anguish at Your lotus feet, —

**70.** why then does my sinful heart not melt?
It is hard as wood; there are no tears in my eyes.

**71.** There is no one more wicked than I:
in separation from You no prema rises in my heart."

**72.** Hearing this, the Lord smiled and embraced Him:
"I shall tell You the truth of it — hear My words.

**73.** Because of Your prema I could not have left —
therefore I keep Your prema tied up in a knot."[^42-9]

**74.** Saying this, He untied the knot of His cloth, —
and the Acharya, thinking of Him, became intoxicated with prema:

**75.** from the ocean of His eyes flowed five, seven streams;
in boundless prema He lost all awareness.

**75+1.** Advaita Prabhu fell, crying "Sri Chaitanya!"[^42-10]
and rolls in the dust in separation from Chaitanya.

**75+2.** Seeing that Advaita could not go on,
the Lord ties the knot again, [binding] His prema.

**76.** Hastily the Lord restrained it,
and then the wise Acharya composed Himself.

**77.** "That is why Your prema does not rise:
because of Your prema I could not go on.

**78.** I am under the sway of Your prema — hear, Acharya:
remember the past and consider Your task."[^42-11]

**79.** Saying this, the Lord went off quickly,
and all the Vaishnavas went to their homes.

**80.** Lochana Dasa tells of Gora's lordly ways:
this is not sannyasa — it is a spear left lodged in the breast.[^42-12]

[^42-1]: The OCR of the summary is badly garbled; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface (ch. 15: "the Lord instructs the devotees to do good to all living beings by chanting the name of Hari; when He prepares to go to Nilachala — Thakura Haridasa's humble words to the Lord; when the devotees begin to follow — He consoles them with the sweetest words and leaves for Nilachala reciting the verse 'Rama Raghava, Rama Raghava, protect me' and others; on the way Nityananda breaks the staff; the Lord shows anger"). The summary runs ahead: the journey to Nilachala and the broken staff are in the next portion.
[^42-2]: "The Lord" here is Jagannatha.
[^42-3]: A sign of utmost humility. Vv. 11–15 — cf. Murari 3.4.28–30.
[^42-4]: The Lord speaks in the mood of a devotee longing for the darshana of Jagannatha.
[^42-5]: A poetic image: the Lord's form is eternal and spiritual, and no one created it; the devotees, in their love, speak of Him as the most beautiful of creations.
[^42-6]: Vv. 47–54 — cf. Murari 3.4.24–26. The Lord kept this promise: the devotees from Bengal came to Him at Puri every year.
[^42-7]: "What you knew before" — that her son is Bhagavan Himself (this is hidden from her by yogamaya, which nourishes her motherly love: en/37, note 16). "Vain" (michha) means "futile", not "unreal" (en/37, note 14).
[^42-8]: A danda is a measure of time, about 24 minutes.
[^42-9]: Cf. Murari 3.4.32–33: Advaita asks why He feels no love on hearing of the Lord's departure, and the Lord replies: "If Your love had shown itself, how could I have left?"
[^42-10]: Vv. 75+1 — 75+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have them.
[^42-11]: Apparently the Lord reminds Advaita — Maha-Vishnu in the form of a devotee (en/27, note 8) — that it was He who by His prayers called the Lord into the world, and of the work for which the Lord came, the spreading of sankirtana.
[^42-12]: In the 1983 edition chapter 15 ends here; the next chapter, "The Journey to Nilachala", begins with v. 81.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 20 (portion 43)

*The section "The Lord's Journey to Nilachala; the Broken Staff" (end): the Lord walks with His companions to Nilachala absorbed in prema, singing the verse "Rama Raghava"; toll-collectors release the pilgrims; Nityananda breaks the Lord's staff and answers His anger*

**81.** Having taken leave of all, the Thakura set out —
and the whole town of Navadvipa was left empty.[^43-1]

**82.** Pandita Sri Gadadhara, the royal Avadhuta,
Narahari and a few others go with Him;

**83.** Srinivasa, Murari, Mukunda, Damodara —
with these His own people the Lord set out.[^43-2]

**84.** "I shall see Jagannatha at the Dola festival," He thought,
and the Lord set off in haste, crying "Hari! Hari!"[^43-3]

**85.** Overwhelmed with prema, the Lord walks along the road;
His body sways, He cannot walk.

**86.** Now He rushes swiftly with the might of a lion,
now He roars aloud and calls out the name of Hari;

**87.** now He dances, now sings, now weeps piteously,
now in the madness of prema He slaps His arms.

**88.** From His reddish eyes streams of tears flow without cease,
and the hair on His whole body stands on end.

**89.** At times He walks slowly and says unearthly things,
at times He stands still and laughs aloud.

**90.** If ever food is brought to Him,
He says, "This is not offered [to Krishna]," and takes nothing.

**91.** Only with great effort [is He persuaded] to accept alms — once in two or three [days]:
out of mercy for people He shows them a lesson.[^43-4]

**92.** All night He stays awake and chants the name of Hari;
aloud He recites this verse, the abode of virtues.

*Thus:*

**93.** "Rama, Raghava, Rama, Raghava, Rama, Raghava, protect me!
Krishna, Keshava, Krishna, Keshava, Krishna, Keshava, save me!"[^43-5]

**94.** This verse the Lord sings in the sweetest voice —
softly, in faltering speech, in the bliss of prema.

**95.** Pilgrims going to see Jagannatha at the Dola festival
walk along with the Lord with joy in their hearts.

**96.** Once, at one place, a crowd of pilgrims
was held up on the road by a toll-collector, sinful and cruel.[^43-6]

**97.** He was giving them much torment and grief.
The Lord, who had gone ahead, quickly turns back.

**98.** The Avadhuta and Gadadhara Pandita are amazed:
"Why has the Lord turned back again?"

**99.** Wondering, they follow behind Him
and a little way off they see: the toll-collector has bound the pilgrims.

**100.** Seeing the reason, they started;
their hair stood on end and they were filled with joy.

**101.** Seeing the pilgrims, the Lord's face grew sad,
and He hurried forward with the gait of an enraged lion.

**102.** Seeing the Lord, the pilgrims weep aloud —
like a frightened child that has reached its mother's lap,

**103.** like wretched forest creatures scorched by a forest fire
that, tormented, plunge into the waters of the Jahnavi.

**104.** The pilgrims weep, fallen at the Lord's feet;
seeing this, the sinful toll-collector thinks to himself:

**105.** "There is no such man in the world!"
He understood in his heart: this is the Moon of Nilachala.[^43-7]

**106.** "I have given them all so much grief —
who knows what He will do? My heart trembles with fear."

**107.** Thinking thus, that great toll-collector
fell at the Lord's feet and speaks piteous words:

**108.** "I have released the pilgrims, I shall not take the toll:
I have understood in my heart, Lord — You are Bhagavan."

**109.** Saying this, he weeps, fallen at His feet,
and [the Lord] placed His lotus feet upon his head.

**110.** Trembling, in a choked voice he offers many praises:
"Do not despise me because I am a worldly man."

**111.** Hearing this, the Lord smiled faintly
and walks on happily, having set the pilgrims free.

**112.** Just then, a little way off, another toll-collector
comes calling out to them with his hand raised.

**113.** Seeing him, the Thakura raised His arm in reply —
and at that sign the toll-collector stopped where he was.

**114.** Tears stream from his eyes, his hair stands on end;
ceaselessly he chants the name "Hare Krishna."

**115.** Seeing the jubilation of Nityananda and Gadadhara,
Lochana Dasa tells the deeds of Gauranga.

*Raga sindhura; disha: "Brothers, sing, sing, hear of the virtues of Gora Gosani! (murchha) Aho, aho, aho! Long for the lotus feet of Gauranga. All that you see in the world you count as your own — ho-ho-ho, ho-ho-ho, brothers — yet all of it is vain forever. Brothers, sing, sing, hear of Gora's virtues!"*[^43-8]

**116.** Thus Gorachand walks along the road,
looking at every holy place He comes upon.

**117.** The Lord goes on, stopping in every village,
and dances at the shrines of the gods.

**118.** Hear of one wondrous thing on the way —
what Nityananda, the king of avadhutas, did.

**119.** Gaurahari gave His staff into Nityananda's hand
and went somewhat ahead, leaving Nityananda behind.

**120.** Overwhelmed with prema, the Lord walks very fast,
forgetting Himself in passionate love for Krishna.

**121.** Gadadhara and all the companions go with Him;
seeing this, Nityananda falls still farther behind.

**122.** Pondering, Prabhu [Nityananda] walks slowly:
"Before my eyes the Lord holds a staff in His hand!

**123.** Leaving that beautiful flute that enchants the three worlds,
He has taken up a staff — how can I bear it?

**124.** The Lord has taken sannyasa and shaved His head —
this cruel pain will stay [with me] all my life."

**125.** The more He pondered, the more His grief grew,
and, setting the staff across His thigh, He broke it.[^43-9]

**126.** Lifting the broken staff, He threw it into the water,
and, afraid of the Lord, walks slowly behind.

**127.** After a while the two came together,
and the Lord asked, "Why do I not see the staff?"

**128.** Shrinking before the Lord, He gives no answer;
the Lord was surprised and grew thoughtful.

**129.** Again the Lord asks, "Where have You put the staff?
Not seeing the staff, I feel great pain in My heart."

**130.** Hearing this, Nityananda Raya says:
"Seeing the staff in Your hand, I burn all over.

**131.** You took sannyasa — that alone, Your shaving Your head, [was bitter];
and more bitter still, You carry a staff on Your shoulder.

**132.** Unable to bear it, I broke it and threw it into the water.
Do as You will," He says in a faltering voice.

**133.** Hearing this, the Lord was grieved
and said angrily, "You do everything backwards!

**134.** In My staff abide all My gods:[^43-10]
what have You gained by breaking such a staff?

**135.** You are always mad, Your mind is unsteady;
Your ways are those of a lunatic, Your intentions a child's.

**136.** Never have You kept the duty that learning requires:
You make [Me] abandon My ashrama — You do everything backwards.

**137.** You see no sin in offending the gods and [violating] the ashrama,
and if I say anything to You, You grow terribly angry."

**138.** Hearing this, Nityananda Prabhu laughs
and answers the Lord in a faltering voice:

**139.** "I do not offend the gods or the ashrama;
whether I did well or ill — You know it all.

**140.** In Your staff abide all Your gods —
and You carry them on Your shoulder: how can I bear it?

**141.** You do them good and I do them harm;
why should I argue with You any further?

**142.** I have offended — forgive my fault this once:
by Your name the whole world is delivered.

**143.** Your name purifies the fallen even more than You Yourself —
so let this offence of mine be forgiven.[^43-11]

**144.** By the name alone the people of the world are delivered —
yet You have taken sannyasa, and the devotees are in great sorrow.

**145.** You shaved Your head with that lovely hair —
a cruel pain has lodged in the devotees' hearts.

**146.** My heart burns ceaselessly seeing this;
whether it is so or not, ask — all the devotees are witnesses.

**147.** For the devotees' grief I broke the staff and threw it away:
it was no staff — it was like a spear in my breast."

**148.** Hearing this, the Lord gave no reply:
His face was sad, but in His heart there was secret joy.[^43-12]

**149.** Nityananda, the great Lord, knows all rasas:
He broke the staff and threw it away, sings Lochana.

[^43-1]: In the 1983 edition chapter 16, "Sri Nilachala-yatra" ("The Journey to Nilachala"), begins here with new numbering; we follow Bhaktisiddhanta's numbering. Bhaktisiddhanta's summary of this section is at the beginning of en/42. The town is left empty because the Lord has left Bengal for good.
[^43-2]: In Murari (3.5.1) the Lord goes "with Mukunda, Gadadhara and others," putting the Avadhuta in front. According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 3), Advaita sent four with the Lord: Nityananda, Jagadananda, Damodara Pandita and Mukunda Datta; Lochana Dasa names the companions differently.
[^43-3]: The Dola-yatra is the spring swing festival of Krishna on the full moon of the month of Phalguna. According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 7), the Lord came to Nilachala in Phalguna and saw the Dola-yatra.
[^43-4]: Vv. 85–92 — cf. Murari 3.5.2–3. As a sannyasi, the Lord teaches people by His own example (loka-shiksha): to accept only food offered to Krishna and to live on alms.
[^43-5]: The same verse is in Murari (3.5.4–5) and in the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 7.96). Bhaktisiddhanta also mentions it in his summary (en/42).
[^43-6]: The toll-collector (dani) took payment from travellers on roads and at ferries. Vv. 96–114 — cf. Murari 3.5.7–9; the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Antya 2) also tells of toll-collectors on the way to Puri.
[^43-7]: The Moon of Nilachala is Jagannatha: the toll-collector recognizes in the Lord the Lord of Nilachala Himself.
[^43-8]: "Vain" (michha) means "futile", not "unreal" (en/37, note 14). The 1983 edition reads "the feet of Radha" instead of "the feet of Gauranga."
[^43-9]: The story of the broken staff is told by Murari (3.5.10–21), in the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Antya 2) and in the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 5): there Nityananda breaks the staff into three pieces and throws it into the river Bhargi. In Murari, Nityananda first excuses Himself by saying he stumbled; in Lochana Dasa He says plainly that he could not bear the sight of the Lord with a sannyasi's staff.
[^43-10]: A sannyasi's staff is held to be the abode of the gods (cf. Murari 3.5.16–17).
[^43-11]: Cf. the teaching that the Lord's name is more merciful than His form (Rupa Gosvami, Namashtaka 6).
[^43-12]: The Lord's anger is lila: in Murari (3.5.18–21) the Lord, setting aside His anger, admits that Nityananda has fulfilled His own intention, and embraces Him; Bhaktisiddhanta, in the edition's table of contents, calls this episode "the manifestation of the pastime of anger" (krodha-lila). Nityananda, the Lord's eternal servant, only carried out His hidden will.

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 21 (portion 44)

*The section "Meeting with Sarvabhauma" (beginning): Tamoluk, Gopala at Remuna, the Vaitarani, Varaha, Yajapura and Viraja; a toll-collector binds Mukunda, and the Lord appears in a dream to his chief; Ekamra (Bhubaneshvara) and the hymn to Shiva; the conversation of Murari and Damodara on the remnants of offerings to Shiva*

## Meeting with Sarvabhauma

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* Sriman Mahaprabhu, passing on the way through Tamoluk, bathing in the Brahma-kunda and seeing Sri Madhusudana, came within a few days to Remuna. There, having seen Sri Gopaladeva, installed by Uddhava, He spent that night absorbed in prema in dancing and singing. The next day, having bathed in the Vaitarani and seen Lord Varaha, He came to the village of Yajapura, saw many lingas of Shiva and joyfully beheld Viraja. There the Lord bathed in the Brahma-kunda, [made offerings to the forefathers at] Nabhi-gaya and from there came to a great holy place, the town of Shiva. [There] a toll-collector mistreated Mukunda, and Gaurahari appeared at night in a dream to the chief of the toll-collectors in the form of the One who lies on the Ocean of Milk and rebuked him for mistreating His devotee; he, frightened, surrendered at the Lord's feet. Then the Lord came from there to Ekamra-kanana, saw ten million lingas of Shiva, Mahesha and Parvati, recited many hymns of praise to Shiva and spent that night there. Next, in the conversation of Murari and Damodara, the question whether Shiva's prasada is worthy of a Vaishnava's respect is settled: it is established that the remnants of offerings to Shiva worshipped by non-devotees are not to be accepted. Then, [passing] Kapoteshvara, He came to the river Bhargavi, bathed there, and, going a little farther and seeing the pinnacle of the temple of Sri Jagannathadeva, fainted in prema; on regaining outer consciousness He bathed again and, offering praises and prayers, came to the house of Sarvabhauma. Sarvabhauma, seeing His form and mahabhava, surmised that He was Bhagavan and told his son to take Him to see Jagannatha. The Lord, standing behind the Garuda pillar, beheld Jagannatha and, absorbed in prema, danced and sang with the devotees; then He was brought from there back to Sarvabhauma's house. After this are described: Sarvabhauma's questions about the Lord, the taking of mahaprasada, the glorification of prasada, another darshana of Jagannatha, Sarvabhauma's [concern] for the Lord's [keeping of] sannyasa, the Lord's questions to Sarvabhauma, the showing of the six-armed form to Sarvabhauma, and so on.[^44-1]

*Raga bhatiyari; disha: "Brothers, sing, O sing the glory and the virtues of Gora Gosani! (murchha) O brothers, my dear brothers, harbour no worldly desires: as long as you live in the world, do not leave the feet of Mahaprabhu!"*

**1.** Thus the Lord walked along the road
and came to Tamoluk, a great holy place.

**2.** Having bathed in the Brahma-kunda and seen Sri Madhusudana,
the Lord is overcome with prema, and His heart rejoices.[^44-2]

**3.** Thus He walked for some days
and came, Mahaprabhu, to the village of Remuna.

**4.** In the great town of Remuna dwells Gopala;
the Lord goes to see Him with boundless joy.

**5.** Formerly He was installed by Uddhava in holy Varanasi,
and He came here to show mercy to a brahmana.[^44-3]

**6.** Saying this, [the Lord] bows again and again
and, crying "Lord of Uddhava!", roars aloud.

**7.** "Today My eyes have borne fruit: I have seen the Lord!"
Through the connection with Uddhava His prema grew immensely.

**8.** With a cry of anguish He calls, "Uddhava! Uddhava!"
and, overwhelmed with prema, falls again and again to the ground and weeps.

**9.** From His reddish eyes tears flow without cease,
His body thrills, and trembling seizes Him again and again.

**10.** Crying "Lord of Uddhava!", He walks around [the Deity]
and dances with His own people, chanting "Hari! Hari!"

**11.** The bliss of prema surged, the jubilation grew;
prema flooded the people, this earth and the sky.

**12.** In joy all the gods look on from the heavens,
with unblinking eyes they gaze at the Lord.

**13.** Indra with his thousand eyes gazes intently:
Gauranga seems to him sweeter than nectar.

**14.** At that very time, on the head
of the Deity Gopala there was a crown of flowers.

**15.** Suddenly the crown slipped from His head,
and, the moment it fell to the ground, [the Lord] picked it up in His hand.[^44-4]

**16.** All around, the Vaishnavas cry "Hari! Hari!";
the waves of prema seem to touch the sky.

**17.** The king of the gods beheld Lord Vishvambhara
and, seeing this wonder, weeps with his head bowed.

**18.** Till the end of the day the Lord dances without rest;
only at evening did the dancing cease.

**19.** Many dishes offered to Krishna
a brahmana brought and set before the Lord.

**20.** Joyful, Mahaprabhu with His own people
happily partook of the mahaprasada.

**21.** He spent the night in the bliss of talks about Krishna
and in the morning set out with His own people.

**22.** Thus the Lord went along the road
and unexpectedly came to the bank of the river Vaitarani.

**23.** Bathing in and drinking [the water of] this river purify the fallen —
and in it the Thakura Himself bathed.

**24.** Then He, the wisest, goes on:
His wish grows to see Lord Varaha,

**25.** seeing whom all people deliver their families on both sides.
Then the Lord goes to the village of Yajapura,

**26.** where Brahma with all the gods performed a sacrifice
and gave the village to the brahmanas by charter.

**27.** Even a great sinner, if he dies in this village,
is freed from all sins and attains the form of Shiva.[^44-5]

**28.** There are hundreds of lingas of Mahesha there;
Gaura-Govinda goes on, bowing to them.

**29.** With joy in His heart He goes to see Viraja;
who can describe the glory of Viraja?

**30.** The sight of her destroys millions of sins;
with a joyful heart the Lord beheld Viraja.

**31.** Bowing to Viraja, He said:
"Give Me prema-bhakti for the feet of Krishna."[^44-6]

**32.** Thus Mahaprabhu went along the road
and here, at Nabhi-gaya, offered pinda to the forefathers.

**33.** He bathed joyfully in the waters of the Brahma-kunda
and, having finished the rites, went on quickly.

**34.** That great holy place is the town of Shiva;
