gazing at it, the Lord was rapt.

**35.** I cannot describe the splendour of that town:
there are millions of lingas, headed by Trilochana.[^44-7]

**36.** At that very time Sri Mukunda Datta,
who knew [the local] ways, says to the Lord:

**37.** "From here on there is nothing more to fear from toll-collectors:
I know them all, I know where each rogue lives."

**38.** Hearing this, the Lord smiled faintly:
"What shall I say to you? You are a man of consequence!

**39.** I have taken up the dharma of sannyasa —
what can a toll-collector do to Me? Tell Me truly."

**40.** Hearing this, Mukunda was not in the least afraid:
"Still, they cause trouble, Lord — I have told You."

**41.** Hearing this, the Thakura says, "Listen, Mukunda:
My body will be guarded by all My kinsfolk."

*Thus in the Shanti-shataka:*

**42.** "He whose father is fortitude, whose mother is forbearance, whose wife is lasting peace,
whose son is truth, whose sister is compassion, whose brother is control of the mind,
whose bed is the ground, whose garment the quarters, whose food the nectar of knowledge —
with such kinsfolk, tell me, friend, what has a yogi to fear?"[^44-8]

**43.** Hearing this, Mukunda felt no fear in his heart.
The Lord, laughing, said to him:

**44.** "You have looked after Me and brought Me this far!"
and with these words He went to beg alms.

**45.** Gadadhara and all the companions
went off to various places to beg alms.

**46.** Then a certain toll-collector held them all up
and in great anger bound Mukunda.

**47.** All day he kept him — his anger did not cool;
only in the evening, with great effort, was he talked round.

**48.** They had just one blanket among them —
and that sinful villain snatched it away.

**49.** In the evening, having begged alms in various places,
they all came one by one to the agreed pavilion.

**50.** The Thakura was already in that pavilion;
seeing Him, all rejoiced greatly.

**51.** Sri Mukunda Datta, fallen at His feet, weeps:
"Till today I did not know Your greatness, Lord!

**52.** Before You I said there was nothing to fear from toll-collectors —
and for that I have suffered so.

**53.** I knew, yet did not know, fool that I am, that You are Bhagavan:
who would take toll from You?

**53+1.** You have no fear in the three worlds:
You are the Lord of the lords of all — who knows You?[^44-9]

**54.** I said it so that You would be without fear —
and it served me right that the toll-collector did this to me."

**55.** Hearing this, the Lord asks Gadadhara,
and he told plainly all that the toll-collector had done.

**56.** Hearing it, the Thakura said, "Do not be upset,"
and spoke just one word: "It will be well."

**57.** That very night, to the chief of the toll-collectors of that region
the son of Shachi appeared in a dream.

**58.** He sees [Him] on the Ocean of Milk, lying on Ananta;
Lakshmi and Sarasvati serve His feet;

**59.** within he sees Sanaka and the others,
and Brahma and the other gods offer praises from afar.

**60.** Seeing this, the king of the toll-collectors trembled in his heart:
seeing [such] majesty, he was dismayed.

**61.** "I am near Viraja in the guise of a sannyasi;
all your servants have wronged My devotee."

**62.** He trembled in his heart, was seized with boundless fear,
and hurried to where Sri Gaura-Gopala was.

**63.** Arriving there shortly, that chief of the toll-collectors
bowed to the Lord and speaks with great humility:

**64.** "You are Bhagavan, who sports in the Ocean of Milk;
to deliver the jivas You have taken sannyasa, Lord.

**65.** You are the moon for the dreadful darkness of worldly existence;
You are the Veda and the utmost bound of the Vedas' highest truth."

**66.** Hearing this, Gorachand said to him with a smile,
"May Krishna soon show you mercy."

**67.** Saying this, He placed His feet upon his head
and, intoxicated with prema, dances with arms raised.

**68.** Having shown him mercy, [the Lord] left him in that land,
taught him devotion to Krishna and gave him the right to it.[^44-10]

**69.** At that time all the Vaishnavas say:
"Your servant caused us much trouble —

**70.** he took away our blanket."
Hearing this, he was embarrassed at heart

**71.** and gave them a new blanket, the chief of the toll-collectors;
then the Vaishnavas' hearts were at peace.

**72.** Then that chief of the toll-collectors, having bowed down,
took his leave and went home.

**73.** Returning home, he took refuge in the service of Krishna
and stays day and night in the sankirtana of Hari's name.

**74.** Thus the whole night passed happily,
and in the morning [the Lord] joyfully performed the morning rites.

**75.** The Lord goes once more to see Viraja,
seeing whom all people cross the ocean of worldly existence.

**76.** Bowing to Viraja, He goes on joyfully;
prema for Krishna rose, His body thrilling.

**77.** The Thakura went on with a lion's might
and little by little came to the village of Ekamra.

**78.** In that village is Shiva with Parvati;
the Lord, beside Himself, hastens to see them.

**79.** From afar the Lord saw the temple —
His longing grew, He was mad with prema.

**80.** Above the temple a beautiful flag is flying;
the whole town of Ekamra is full of Shiva-lingas.

**81.** Seeing the flag, the Lord bowed down
and little by little entered the town of Shiva.

**82.** Ten million lingas are in the town of Ekamra:
as one walks, the heart trembles with fear.[^44-11]

**83.** Vishveshvara and the rest — millions of lingas;
the very soil of the town looks like sandesh.

**84.** In the Maha-Bindu-sarovara is the water of all holy places,
and many other holy tirthas are in the town.

**85.** Entering the town, [the Lord] sees Parvati and Shankara
and, bowing down, is rapt in prema.

**86.** All beheld Parvati and Mahesha:
the sight of the linga ended everyone's afflictions.

**87.** Seeing Mahesha, the Lord lost control of His body:
His body sways, He cannot stand steady.

**88.** From His reddish eyes tears flow without cease,
His cheeks thrill — again and again He recites a hymn.

*Thus [the hymn]:*

**88+1.** "Obeisance, obeisance to you forever — to the lord of the gods, the master of the bhutas, the Gracious One (Mrida), whose crest is the young moon washed by the waves of the Ganga, the festival of Gauri's eyes;
to him whose faces shine like molten gold, the moon, the blue lotus, coral and the rain-cloud, who in the arena of the beautiful dance grants the desired boons — to the lord of kaivalya, whose banner is the bull!"[^44-12]

**89.** Thus Mahaprabhu recites a hymn to Shiva,
and all around Him all the Vaishnavas recite hymns.

**90.** At that time Shiva's servants
offered the Lord fragrance, sandal paste and a garland.

**91.** Bowing to Shiva, the Lord came outside
and, entering a house, rested.

**92.** He ate food offered by a devotee
and, tired from the road, lay down for the night.

**92+1.** At the hour of sleep, meditating on the lotus feet of Krishna,
He then thinks in His heart:

**92+2.** "If by good fortune I were to receive Shiva's mahaprasada,
I would partake of it — such is My hope."[^44-13]

**92+3.** Just as Mahaprabhu thought this,
someone says, "Take this prasada — pana."

**92+4.** The Thakura rose and took the prasada, the pana;
drinking the pana, He was filled with happiness.

**92+5.** What was left He gave to His own people,
and all the devotees partook of it with special reverence.

**93.** Thus in bliss He spent that night;
at dawn the Lord rose, the Master of the three worlds.

**94.** Having done the morning rites and bathed in the Bindu-sarovara,
the Thakura set out, bowing to Maheshvara.

**95.** His own people went with the Lord.
In this connection I shall tell a story.

**96.** What was said between Murari and Damodara —
listen, all of you, carefully: I shall tell it now.

**97.** Pandita Damodara asked Murari:
"Why did the Lord accept the remnants of offerings to Shiva?

**98.** Because of Bhrigu's curse, the remnants of offerings to Shiva are not to be accepted —
why then did the Lord Himself accept them?

**99.** This Mahaprabhu is Himself Brahmanya-deva:
how could He knowingly transgress [the rule]?"[^44-14]

**100.** Murari replies, "Listen, listen, Damodara:
what do I know of the Lord's inner answer?

**101.** I shall answer what I infer by my own understanding —
if your mind accepts it, keep it in your heart.

**102.** A servant of Shiva who serves Shiva
but does not accept [Hari's] remnants, making a difference between Hari and Hara —

**103.** to him the brahmana's curse applies; this is the point:
his mind is impure, he does not know [Shiva's] greatness.

**104.** But one who serves without dividing them
partakes of the remnants of offerings to Shiva.

**105.** The remnants of offerings to Shiva are eaten by one whose conduct makes no division:
in such a one there is greater love for both Hari and Hara.

**106.** Maheshvara is the king of all Vaishnavas;
whoever worships him seeing him thus —

**107.** from that person's hand Shiva partakes [of the offering];
eat such prasada, and bondage falls away.

**108.** In truth, that Maheshvara, as the Lord passed by,
offered Him hospitality with great joy in his heart.

**109.** Curses and the like, listen, are for those turned away [from the Lord];
but when [Shiva] is worshipped as [Hari's] friend, love for Sri Krishna arises.[^44-15]

**110.** The Lord descended to teach the people."
Damodara says, "Now the knot is untied!"

**111.** Hearing this, all the people rejoice at heart.
Lochana Dasa tells of the deeds of Chaitanya.

[^44-1]: The OCR of the summary is garbled in places; gaps are filled by sense and from the table of contents in the edition's preface (ch. 16). "Concern for the keeping of sannyasa" is a conjectural reading (OCR «অন্গটাস-সংরগণ-চিস্ত।»). The summary covers the whole section, whose end is in en/45. In the 1983 edition this section continues chapter 16 ("Sri Nilachala-yatra"), with new numbering.
[^44-2]: Vv. 1–31 — cf. Murari 3.6. Tamoluk (Tamolipta, Tamralipta) is present-day Tamluk in Bengal.
[^44-3]: So too in Murari (3.6.4): the Gopala of Remuna was once installed in Varanasi by Uddhava and came here to show mercy to a brahmana. In the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 4) the Deity of Remuna is Gopinatha Kshirachora, while the story of Gopala who followed a brahmana is told of Sakshi-gopala (Madhya 5).
[^44-4]: Cf. Murari 3.6.8–9: the crown fell from the Deity's head, and the Lord caught it.
[^44-5]: So says the mahatmya (glorification) of the holy place; likewise in Murari (3.6.16). For a Vaishnava the highest fruit of pilgrimage is not such rewards but prema — which is what the Lord asks of Viraja (v. 31).
[^44-6]: The Lord, playing the part of a devotee, asks of the goddess only love for Krishna (cf. Murari 3.6.18) — as the gopis prayed to Katyayani for Krishna.
[^44-7]: Trilochana ("the Three-eyed") is a linga of Shiva; the town is compared to Varanasi (cf. Murari 3.6.20). According to Murari this is Yajapura; Ekamra (Bhubaneshvara) comes below, v. 77.
[^44-8]: A verse from the Shanti-shataka. In A it is followed by Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya and Bengali translation (OCR garbled); the text of the verse is restored from B. Vv. 36–73 — cf. Murari 3.7.
[^44-9]: V. 53+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition does not have it.
[^44-10]: The dream vision is Lochana Dasa's; in Murari (3.7.12) the chief of the toll-collectors simply comes to the Lord.
[^44-11]: For fear of stepping on a linga.
[^44-12]: V. 88+1 (the hymn to Shiva) is found only in the 1983 edition and in the 1903 edition; Bhaktisiddhanta's edition does not have it. These are verses 3.8.13–14 of Murari Gupta's poem. The Lord, playing the part of a devotee, honours Shiva as the greatest of Vaishnavas and prays to him for love of Hari (Murari 3.8).
[^44-13]: Vv. 92+1 — 92+5 are found only in the 1983 edition and (with variants) in the 1903 edition. Cf. Murari 3.9: the Lord desires Shiva's prasada and, receiving it, partakes of it with reverence. Pana is a sweet cooling drink.
[^44-14]: Brahmanya-deva — "the God who favours brahmanas": He does not break a brahmana's word. Bhrigu's curse on the worshippers of Shiva — Bhagavata Purana 4.2. The conversation follows Murari 3.9 (Murari telling the story to Damodara Pandita).
[^44-15]: "Without division" (abheda) here is not a mayavada identification: Shiva is not worshipped as a separate god independent of Krishna and His rival, but honoured as the best of Vaishnavas (Bhagavata Purana 12.13.16), inseparable from the Lord (cf. Brahma-samhita 5.45: Shambhu is to the Lord as curd is to milk). Bhaktisiddhanta in his summary states the conclusion thus: the remnants of offerings to Shiva worshipped by non-devotees are not to be accepted (cf. vv. 102–103, 109).

---

# Madhya-khanda, part 22 (portion 45)

*The section "Meeting with Sarvabhauma" (end): Kapoteshvara and the Bhargavi; the boy with the flute above the temple; Jagannatha stretches out His hands to the Lord; the Lord at Sarvabhauma's house and the first darshana of Jagannatha; mahaprasada and its glory; Sarvabhauma censures the Lord and offers to teach Him Vedanta; the Lord reveals to him the essence of Vedanta and shows the six-armed form; the author's word; the end of the Madhya-khanda*

*Disha: "Sing the sweet name of Sri Krishna Chaitanya — Gorachand! (murchha) Brothers, there is no other way to be saved: this story is rare in the world. As long as you live in the world, drink it, filling your ears; never give up the songs of His virtues!"*[^45-1]

**112.** Now hear again of the deeds of Gorachand:
the Lord pours down a rain of prema — new nectar.

**113.** The Lord walks along the road with His own people
and with great joy sees Kapoteshvara.

**114.** Bowing to him, the Lord walks on,
looking at holy places and great lingas.

**115.** Then comes a river named Bhargavi, the blessed;
in it He bathed together with His own people.

**116.** Having finished His bath, the Lord walks on
and suddenly saw the temple of Jagannatha.[^45-2]

**117.** The temple shines, outshining the rays of the moon,
and on it a red flag flutters in the wind.

**118.** In the midst of the Blue Hill is the beautiful temple of Hari:
wondrously white, it outshines Kailasa with its brilliance.

**119.** A boy dark as collyrium
the Lord plainly sees above the temple.

**120.** With His hand holding a cloth He beckons Him again and again;
seeing this, the Lord, rapt, bows to Him.

**121.** The Lord fell to the ground unconscious,
lying silent, as if life had left Him.

**122.** Seeing this, all the people were stricken at heart;
they call, "Lord! Lord!" — He does not answer.

**123.** "What has happened? What has happened?" they wonder in alarm,
and no sound comes from them: as if alive, yet dead.

**124.** At that very moment the Lord quickly rose,
His whole body thrilling, intoxicated with prema.

**125.** Seeing this, all the people came back to life —
as if life had returned to a dead body.

**126.** Mahaprabhu asks them,
"Do you see anything with your eyes above the temple?

**127.** Radiant as the rays of a blue gem,
a beautiful boy who enchants the three worlds?"

**128.** Seeing nothing, they say, "We see,"
fearing that He might faint again.

**129.** Again the Lord says to them,
"Look: on the temple's banner — a beautiful boy!

**130.** His face is gracious, His form like full nectar;
wondrously the fingers of His palms are moving.

**131.** He beckons Me with the beauty of His lotus hand;
in His left hand is a flute, the blessing of the three worlds."[^45-3]

**132.** Saying this, the Lord hurried forward,
and all the Vaishnavas follow joyfully.

**133.** The radiance of Gaura's body outshines millions of moons;
His long tilaka of sandal paste gleams.

**134.** On Gora's body the reddish cloth glows bright;
its colour outshines the morning sun.

**135.** Seeing the temple of Jagannatha, Gora Raya
bows again and again and walks on.

**136.** Tears flow from His eyes without cease,
and the hair on His whole body stands on end.

**137.** Overwhelmed with prema, with an eager heart,
the Lord came to the great tirtha, the lake of Markandeya.

**138.** Having bathed and given gifts as custom prescribes,
He bowed down and hurried on.

**139.** Bowing to Yajneshvara, full of joy,
with eagerness in His heart He walks on quickly.

**140.** Seeing again the temple of Jagannatha,
He bows again, falling to the ground.

**141.** From both His eyes tears flow without cease;
rapt, He weeps in deep longing.

**142.** Seeing such longing in Gorachand,
Jagannatha appeared to Him, stretching out His hands.[^45-4]

**143.** "Come, come!" calls the King of the three worlds;
seeing this, the Lord, rapt, rolls on the ground.

**144.** Laughing with joy, He said,
"Be merciful, Jagannatha — I have seen Your feet!"

**145.** When He no longer sees [Him], He weeps again,
and seeing [Him] again, He is filled with exultation.

**146.** Nothing but wild surges of prema, His body thrilling;
with a thunderous roar the waves of the nectar of prema rise.

**147.** Then the Lord went on in the same haste
and came to the house of Vasudeva Sarvabhauma.

**148.** Sarvabhauma, joyfully seeing the Lord,
offered Him a seat as householders' custom requires.

**148+1.** He bowed, saying "Namo narayanaya";
[the Lord replied,] "May your mind soon turn to Radha and Krishna."[^45-5]

**148+2.** Hearing the Lord's words of blessing, the Bhattacharya
understood: this is a Vaishnava sannyasi, a great acharya.

**149.** The Lord said to Sarvabhauma,
"I long to see Jagannatha.

**150.** How shall I see the Lord, the God of gods?
To stand before Him — My heart is in awe."

**151.** Hearing this, the venerable Sarvabhauma
gazes in wonder at the Lord's form:

**152.** "He is golden like molten gold, beautiful as Sumeru;
His face, a moon with lovely eyes, is shining.

**153.** A lion's neck, a conch-like throat, long eyes,
arms reaching to the knees — all auspicious marks."

**153+1.** In bright prema for Krishna He is overcome with longing,
thrilling all over, His body sways.[^45-6]

**154.** Seeing this, Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya, overwhelmed,
began to ponder, seeing all these wonders:

**155.** "There is no such man in all the world;
not even among the gods can I place Him.

**156.** The Lord of Vaikuntha has come Himself:
this is that Bhagavan — so I conclude."

**157.** Thinking thus, the great Sarvabhauma,
seeing his son, says to him:[^45-7]

**158.** "Go quickly with Chaitanya;
listen carefully to what this great soul says.

**159.** Where Sri Jagannatha Mahaprabhu dwells —
take Him with His companions there, to Him."

**160.** Hearing this, Gora Raya was pleased
and went, guided by Sarvabhauma's son.

**161.** At the Lion Gate the Lord's body swayed:
rapt in prema, He cannot keep on His feet.

**162.** He cannot walk steadily, His limbs have gone limp;
all the companions walk carefully close beside Him.

**163.** With great effort He entered the Lion Gate
and at once hurried up into the Nata-mandira.

**164.** Standing behind Garuda, He gazes intently —
and saw the moon-face of the King of the three worlds.[^45-8]

**165.** His heart leapt up, filled with bliss;
His body was covered with thrills, like a kadamba blossom.

**166.** Five, seven streams of tears flow from His eyes;
He forgot Himself, overpowered by the bliss of prema.

**167.** The Lord fell to the ground, His body helpless —
as if the wind had toppled a peak of Sumeru.

**168.** In the rapture of prema Bhagavan fainted:
both hands clenched tight in fists, eyes closed.

**168+1.** His cloth slipped on His senseless body;
seeing this, the brahmanas went out of the temple.

**168+2.** Leaving His throne, Lord Jagannatha lifted [Him] up;
at each other's touch both were filled with delight.

**168+3.** Putting His arms around [Him], He at once embraced Him,
and [the Lord] dances before Jagannatha, crying "Haribol!"

**168+4.** At Gauranga's touch Jagannatha, intoxicated with prema,
seated Gora upon His throne.[^45-9]

**169.** The son of Shachi dances, crying "Hari!" —
and then all entered the temple.

**170.** Gadadhara dances, and Narahari, Nityananda,
Srinivasa, Damodara, Murari, Mukunda;

**171.** all the other devotees too dance in joy,
making kirtana, singing the virtues of Radha and Kanu.

**172.** Then all the companions, after deliberating,
brought the Lord to Sarvabhauma's house.[^45-10]

**173.** In Sarvabhauma's house the Lord came to Himself
and began to dance again in the kirtana of [Krishna's] virtues.

**174.** Seeing this, Sarvabhauma Vasudeva Bhattacharya
felt great delight in his heart, seeing this wonder.

**175.** When Mahaprabhu's dancing was over,
Sarvabhauma invited Him to accept alms.

**176.** He sent brahmanas to bring prasada,
and he, Sarvabhauma, converses with the Lord.

**177.** He holds a friendly conversation to learn His learning
and began to ask the Lord something about Himself:

**178.** "Where were You born? Tell me."
The Lord says, "What you have said is true."

**179.** The Bhattacharya says, "What are You saying?
I say one thing and You another — why?"

**180.** The Lord stays silent, deep as the ocean;
again the grave brahmana asks the Lord:

**181.** "Who are Your mother and father? Tell me."
The Lord says, "What you have said is true."

**182.** The Bhattacharya asks yet again,
"Tell me, where did You take sannyasa?"

**183.** The Lord says, "Know for certain: this is true."
Hearing this, Sarvabhauma was greatly amazed.

**184.** He could not at all understand the Lord's resolve —
the Lord of myriad Sarasvatis, the victory of all.[^45-11]

**185.** "Is He God — or of a madman's nature?"
Only vexation and anger fell to his lot.

**186.** The Bhattacharya had much prasada brought;
at the sight of prasada the madness of prema arose.

**187.** Receiving the mahaprasada — Jagannatha's rice —
the Lord, laughing, reverently raised it to His head.

**188.** He gave a roar in a deep voice —
that lion's roar of the Lord filled the universe.

**189.** Gods, gandharvas, men, jackals, dogs
and all the clans of the nagas came to Gauranga.

**190.** [He] joyfully put that prasada into all their mouths —
Gadadhara and the others and Prabhu Nityananda saw it.

**191.** No one said anything: all knew the truth.
All received prasada together with the devotees.

**192.** He ate a little with His own people;
then Srinivasa spoke:

**193.** "I have a request, Lord, but I am afraid to say it;
I shall ask without fear, if I have Your leave.

**194.** When You received the prasada and laughed,
I was startled to see it. Explain this to me."

**195.** Hearing this, the Lord, in great joy,
speaks, revealing what is hidden:

**196.** "By Katyayani's vow, such a treasure as prasada
is eaten by jackals and dogs — listen, brahmana.[^45-12]

**197.** Indra, Chandra, the gandharvas or the gods —
for all it is a rarity: not to be had even by effort.

**198.** Narada, Prahlada, Shuka and the other devotees —
even for them it is rare: I have told you the secret.

**199.** Such mahaprasada all are eating —
that is the secret I have told you, what was in My heart.

**200.** Whoever, having received such mahaprasada,
takes it for ordinary food or does not eat it —

**201.** even the dharma he gathered in former births perishes,
and he is born from the womb of a sow.

**202.** Even if [prasada] falls from a dog's mouth,
eat it as soon as you get it — there is no fault in this at all."[^45-13]

**203.** Then Mahaprabhu reverently accepted the alms
and in the evening goes to see Jagannatha.

**204.** Entering the temple, He sees the beautiful face;
the universe cannot contain His inner delight.

**204+1.** Incense, lamps, lovely flowers, enchanting fragrance
the brahmana offered, seeing [His] bliss.

**204+2.** Seeing the dazzling splendour of [Jagannatha's] body —
as if moons by the hundred thousand had gathered in one place —[^45-14]

**205.** the radiance of His body, outshining a fresh cloud,
and in it two wondrous lotus eyes,

**206.** the Thakura plunged into an ocean of bliss;
He rolls on the ground, His prema grew immensely.

**207.** His long body, like Mount Sumeru,
rolls on the ground, unable to contain the bliss.

**208.** By Gauranga's radiance Jagannatha became golden,
His body filled with bhava, utterly rapt.

**209.** Balarama too became golden, and the pandas;
the bodies of all were filled with bhava.

**210.** They raised Gauranga up, and a panda performed arati:
beside the unmoving Brahman — the moving form.[^45-15]

**211.** Jagannatha appeared in the form of a sannyasi —
such a wonder no one had ever seen.

**212.** After a while He came to Himself
and returned to His lodging with His own people.

**213.** Thus, seeing Jagannatha three times [a day],
He knows neither day nor night — an ocean of bliss.

**214.** Thus the Lord, expert in prema, spends some days
joyfully with His own people.

**215.** Now hear carefully the story
of how He first revealed Himself at Purushottama.

**216.** The Lord teaches the people, having become destitute;
not understanding this, fools take Him for a man.

**217.** By the sea Gaura Raya made Himself a garden
and there with His own people sings His own virtues.[^45-16]

**218.** Sarvabhauma, his mind deluded by learning,
said something mistaken about the Lord behind His back.

**219.** In an assembly full of brahmanas and worthy men,
that brahmana spoke what was in his heart:

**220.** "This sannyasi is of a noble family, a great scholar;
but it is not proper to take sannyasa at a young age.

**221.** At this time of life the dharma of sannyasa is unfitting;
not understanding this, the brahmana has done such a thing.

**222.** Let Him undergo His purificatory rites again,
learn Vedanta and keep the rule of an ashrama.

**223.** Kirtana and dancing are not a sannyasi's dharma;
let Him hear Vedanta from me."[^45-17]

**223+1.** "As many times as Jagannatha eats,
so many times this sannyasi eats too.

**223+2.** One who eats so much in youth —
how can his lust ever subside?

**223+3.** He remembers home — and weeps, 'Radha!'
To his misfortune the sannyasi has fallen into the noose of sannyasa."

**223+4.** Meanwhile Gorachand was with His own people
in talks about Krishna, in conversation on prema.[^45-18]

**224.** Suddenly Gora Panhu smiled faintly —
as if honey had poured down in unbroken streams.

**225.** Knowing everything, the Lord went there
where Sarvabhauma sits teaching Vedanta.

**226.** He came there with His own people;
seeing Him, the Bhattacharya rose in alarm.

**227.** He offered Him a seat with respectful words,
and the Thakura asks him for guidance: "What should I do?

**228.** You, Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya, know everything;
I ask you from My heart — tell Me the rule.

**229.** I do not understand the dharma of the sannyasa ashrama;
I have taken sannyasa — you judge what the law requires.

**230.** You know all truths, you expound Vedanta —
what is the rule? Teach Me now.

**231.** At a young age the dharma of sannyasa is not proper:
what is enjoined — to undergo the sacred-thread rite again?

**231+1.** Jagannatha's prasada has made Me drunk,
and in youth I cannot quell My lust.

**231+2.** I remember home — and weep, saying 'Radha!' —
that is why in the midst of kirtana I am beside Myself."[^45-19]

**232.** Hearing this, Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya
was abashed at heart and wonders:

**233.** "Only just now I said this with my disciples —
how did this sannyasi come to know all this?"

**234.** Guessing [the truth], tormented by shame,
he said nothing and remained in wonder.

**235.** The next day the Lord went with His own people
to Sarvabhauma's house to see him.

**236.** Sarvabhauma, sitting at home, is teaching Vedanta;
the Lord, smiling, asks about the conclusions of Vedanta.

**237.** The Thakura asked about the hidden meaning of Vedanta —
about refuge at the feet of Krishna, the sprout of nectar.[^45-20]

**237+1.** "The Veda declares that Brahman has a form like a man's —
yet you do not accept this, relying on your own mind.

**237+2.** Brahma's word in the Brahma-samhita says:
He is eternal being, consciousness and bliss, full of supreme majesty;

**237+3.** His body is made of rasa, His form is dark;
the other avataras are portions — Krishna is the complete, the highest.

**237+4.** Vyasa declared this in the Bhagavata —
yet you reject it and teach another doctrine.

**237+5.** The Varaha-samhita says: Radha is the complete truth;
all other prakritis are the radiance of Her toenails.

**237+6.** In the Gautamiya-tantra and the Sanatkumara-samhita
the truth of Radha is set forth.

**237+7.** The sage Vyasa wrote down the meaning of the Veda in the shastras:
what do you gain by reviling Vyasa?

**237+8.** The abode of Vrindavana is Krishna's land of chintamani;
there Krishna sports with His beloveds.

**237+9.** The crown of the beloveds is Radha, the great goddess;
the great truth is Lord Krishna, realized in the Vedas.

**237+10.** The kirtana of the two is sung by all the gopis —
and you revile that kirtana: you are base.

**237+11.** The Bhagavata speaks of the glory of kirtana:
the killing of a brahmana and other sins — all are destroyed.

**237+12.** So the name destroys mountains of sin,
and then [one] attains Krishna: the name is called chintamani.

**237+13.** Taste prasada, and millions of sins are destroyed —
yet you call it the display of greed, delusion and lust!

**237+14.** The glory of the Vaishnavas is proved by all the shastras;
you do not accept the shastras — what knowledge of the shastras do you have?"[^45-21]

**238.** Hearing this, Sarvabhauma was amazed at heart:
he understood that the son of Shachi is not a man.

**239.** Shame tormented him, fear was in his heart:
"In all these years I have never heard such an essence!

**240.** All that I have read and heard through so many years
I have taught my disciples with pride.

**241.** And now I have heard this conclusion of Vedanta:
this Mahaprabhu is the Lord of Sarasvati Himself."

**242.** Concluding thus, Sarvabhauma, king of brahmanas,
with joined palms offers praises, seeing this.

**243.** At that very moment the Lord [showed] a six-armed form;
seeing it, Sarvabhauma lost all composure in bliss.

**244.** In the upper two hands He holds bow and arrow,
in the middle two the flute at His lips,

**245.** in the lower two the staff and the kamandalu.
Seeing this, Sarvabhauma was beside himself with bliss.[^45-22]

**246.** Fallen at His feet, he weeps in great humility;
Sarvabhauma offers praises in a choked voice.

**247.** In a choked voice he recites a thousand praises —
people know them as the "Thousand Names of Chaitanya."[^45-23]

**247+1.** "Glory to Raghuvira, Yaduvira, the Great One!
Glory to the hero among brahmanas, the Lion Gaura, the refuge of all!

**247+2.** Drunk with the pride of learning I reviled You —
now I have sold myself to Your feet, which grant fearlessness.

**247+3.** Forgive my offence! Glory to Gaurahari!
You are supremely merciful to all."

**247+4.** Gaura, the great lion, showed mercy to Sarvabhauma;
the bliss of all the devotees grew — great bees.[^45-24]

**248.** Rapt, he falls at the lotus feet.
Lochana tells how [the Lord] revealed Himself to Sarvabhauma.

**249.** Thus the Lord stays in bliss and delight,
and the people of Nilachala behold Him with joy.

**250.** The glory of Jagannatha shone forth more than before;
in everyone's heart happiness touches the sky.

**251.** Who knows how to tell the story of Chaitanya?
I cannot hold back — I tell a little with my lips.

**252.** Sri Murari Gupta the vaidya, blessed in the three worlds —
Pandita Sri Damodara asked him;

**253.** Murari Gupta told it in verses —
what I have heard is by the grace of them both.[^45-25]

**254.** Hearing it, my heart was stirred with longing for its sweetness,
and, not seeing my own faults, my mind was enraptured.

**255.** What I have told according to my own understanding
I, a wretched fool, tell in a sung panchali.

**256.** The Sutra-khanda, Adi-khanda and Madhya-khanda are ended;
the Shesha-khanda remains — I shall tell it in the story.

**257.** The story of Chaitanya's deeds is the manifestation of Chaitanya.
"The Madhya-khanda is ended," says this Lochana Dasa.

*Thus ends the Madhya-khanda of the Sri Chaitanya-mangala, composed by Sri Lochana Dasa Thakura.*[^45-26]

[^45-1]: The name of the raga is illegible in all the editions. The 1983 edition begins new numbering here (१).
[^45-2]: Vv. 113–146 — cf. Murari 3.10. Kapoteshvara (the Kapota-linga) is a shrine of Shiva on the way from Bhubaneshvara to Puri; the Bhargavi is a river near Puri.
[^45-3]: As in Murari (3.10): above the temple Krishna appears to the Lord as a boy with a flute; His companions do not see Him.
[^45-4]: Cf. Murari 3.10.17–18: Jagannatha mercifully shows the Lord His hand.
[^45-5]: Vv. 148+1 — 148+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Cf. the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6): Sarvabhauma greets the Lord with "namo narayanaya," and the Lord blesses him: "May your mind be fixed on Krishna." The 1983 edition reads v. 147: "Overwhelmed with prema, with an eager heart, the Lord came to the house of Vasudeva Sarvabhauma."
[^45-6]: V. 153+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives it only in a footnote, as an additional reading of two manuscripts.
[^45-7]: In Murari (3.11) it is Sarvabhauma's younger brother who goes with the Lord to the temple; one of the manuscripts of the 1903 edition also reads "younger brother." According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6), Sarvabhauma himself found the Lord unconscious in the temple and brought Him to his house.
[^45-8]: The Garuda pillar stands in the Nata-mandira (the dancing hall) facing Jagannatha; according to tradition, the Lord always beheld Jagannatha standing behind it.
[^45-9]: Vv. 168+1 — 168+4 are found only in the 1983 edition and in the 1903 edition (in the main text, in brackets); Bhaktisiddhanta's edition does not have them. The other biographies do not tell this: according to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6), the Lord rushed to embrace Jagannatha and fell unconscious.
[^45-10]: So too in Murari (3.11) and the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6): the unconscious Lord was carried to Sarvabhauma's house.
[^45-11]: Sarasvati is the goddess of learning and speech; the Lord is the Master of all knowledge (en/17, note 1). The Lord evades questions about Himself, concealing who He is.
[^45-12]: On the vow of Katyayani (Parvati), by which Vishnu's mahaprasada goes to all — see the Sutra-khanda (en/04, vv. 232–272).
[^45-13]: A hyperbole glorifying mahaprasada: nothing can defile its purity. On the glory of mahaprasada cf. Murari 3.11.
[^45-14]: Vv. 204+1 — 204+2 are found only in the 1983 edition and in the 1903 edition; Bhaktisiddhanta's edition does not have them.
[^45-15]: "The unmoving Brahman" is Jagannatha, Daru-brahma (the Supreme in a form of wood), not the impersonal Brahman; Gauranga is the same Jagannatha, but moving. Balarama is Baladeva, Jagannatha's elder brother on the altar; the pandas are the temple priests.
[^45-16]: That is, Krishna's virtues: He Himself is Krishna. The garden (tota) by the sea is apparently the later Tota-Gopinatha.
[^45-17]: Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya, the greatest master of logic and Vedanta of his time, then held the doctrine of advaita (mayavada): for him sannyasa was renunciation for the study of Vedanta, and kirtana and dancing were unworthy of a sannyasi. Lochana Dasa plainly calls this the error of a mind "deluded by learning" (v. 218). Cf. Murari 3.12 (Sarvabhauma wants to perform the sacred-thread rite on the Lord anew and teach Him Vedanta) and the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6). Sarvabhauma's conversion from dry knowledge to bhakti is the heart of this lila.
[^45-18]: Vv. 223+1 — 223+4 are found only in the 1983 edition and in the 1903 edition; Bhaktisiddhanta's edition does not have them. This is the slander of the deluded Sarvabhauma, a worldly reading of what is beyond the world: the Lord, Krishna Himself in the mood of Radha, weeps in separation from Krishna; the taking of mahaprasada is not eating for the body. Later Sarvabhauma repents (v. 247+2).
[^45-19]: Vv. 231+1 — 231+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. The Lord ironically repeats Sarvabhauma's words, showing that He knows them.
[^45-20]: As in Murari (3.12): the true, "hidden" meaning of Vedanta is refuge at the feet of Krishna. According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6), the Lord, after listening silently for seven days to Sarvabhauma's exposition, explained the Vedanta-sutra in its direct sense, rejecting the impersonal interpretation, and revealed the meaning of the atmarama verse.
[^45-21]: Vv. 237+1 — 237+14 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Their content agrees with Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, but they are apparently late: according to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 9), the Lord found the Brahma-samhita later, on His pilgrimage in the South. "The name is chintamani" — cf. the Padma Purana: "The name of Krishna is chintamani, Krishna Himself." Chintamani is the wish-fulfilling gem.
[^45-22]: The bow and arrow are Rama's, the flute Krishna's, the staff and kamandalu those of Gaura the sannyasi. Lochana Dasa has described the six-armed form before (en/28, v. 101). According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6), the Lord showed Sarvabhauma first a four-armed form and then the two-armed form of Krishna with the flute; the six-armed form shown to Sarvabhauma is honoured by tradition.
[^45-23]: A hundred verses in praise of the Lord (the Chaitanya-shataka) are ascribed to Sarvabhauma; two of them are quoted in the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 6.254–255; cf. Murari 3.12.17–18).
[^45-24]: Vv. 247+1 — 247+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^45-25]: Lochana Dasa names his source: the Sanskrit poem of Murari Gupta, framed as Murari's narration to Damodara Pandita.
[^45-26]: In the 1983 edition chapter 16 and the Madhya-khanda end here.

---

# Shesha-khanda, part 1 (portion 46)

*The beginning of the Shesha-khanda: the Lord sets out for the South; the brahmanas Kurma and Vasudeva at Kurma-kshetra; at Jiyada-Nrisimha the Lord tells the tale of the cowherd Punda who wounded the Boar, of the Deity rising from the earth as milk was poured, and of the merchant Jiyada, after whose name Nrisimha is called; the meeting with Ramananda Raya at Kanchi: the Lord appears to him in meditation and reveals His form of Krishna*

## The Lord's Journey to the South

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* Sriman Mahaprabhu, after staying some time in Puri in the bliss of kirtana together with Sarvabhauma, went to the southern lands to see Setubandha. From there, having shown mercy at Kurma-kshetra to a certain brahmana named Vasudeva and having instructed him that the dharma of the Kali age is the holy name of Sri Hari alone, He arrived at Jiyada-Nrisimha. Here, in the course of the narrative, the author relates the ancient history of Jiyada-Nrisimha.
>
> From Jiyada-Nrisimha Sriman Mahaprabhu came to Kanchi-nagara, to Ramananda, and, having shown him the form of Rasaraja and Mahabhava[^46-1], went by way of the Godavari to Panchavati; there, absorbed in prema, He saw all the places where Ramachandra had performed His various lilas during His exile in the forest, and came to Sri Ranganatha on the bank of the Kaveri. There He showed mercy to Trimalla Bhatta and spent the period of chaturmasya in his house. After that He met Paramananda Puri, the disciple of Madhavendra Puri-pada. Remembering the prophecy about the descent of Sriman Mahaprabhu [that he had heard] from the lips of Madhavendra Puri-pada, Paramananda Puri understood that the Lord was Bhagavan Himself and offered Him many hymns and prayers.

**1.** Glory to You, Lord of the life of Narahari and Gadadhara!
Be merciful, O Lord — cast on us Your auspicious glance[^46-2].

**2.** I shall tell the story of the Shesha-khanda — the essence of nectar:
in one who hears it a shoreless ocean of happiness grows.

**3.** Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya offered praises [to the Lord],
and for some days [the Lord] spent day and night in kirtana.

**4.** To see Setubandha the Thakura set out [on His way];
in a town called Kurma He sees a brahmana named Kurma[^46-3].

**5.** In the same village lives a brahmana named Vasudeva —
He met them both in one place.

**6.** By the sight of the Lord they became pure;
they gaze at Gora's body, overwhelmed with prema.

**7.** His body is beautiful as Sumeru, His arms reach to the knees,
His neck is a lion's, His throat like a conch, His eyes long.

**8.** As they gazed, the joy in their hearts kept growing,
and they knew for certain: this Gaurachandra is Krishna.

**9.** Crying "O Mahaprabhu!" they fell at His feet;
all the people weep [hearing] their weeping of prema.

**10.** Raising them both, the Lord embraced them
and revealed to them sweet words:

**11.** "Listen, listen, O brahmanas, to My word:
for what have you come into the world, what do you follow?

**12.** In the Kali age the dharma is the sankirtana of Hari's name:
Krishna has revealed the great treasure of the name.

**13.** Rejoice in singing the names and qualities [of the Lord];
dance, dance — let people be freed from bondage!"[^46-4]

**14.** Having said this, the Lord quickly went away,
and they lost all awareness of themselves.

**15.** He cannot walk along the road, so does the rapture of prema grow;
going on a little, He sees Jiyada-Nrisimha[^46-5].

**16.** "I shall tell an ancient story, a wondrous tale," —
so, overwhelmed with prema, He Himself began the account.

**17.** "Listen, all people, to a joyful secret —
how Jiyada-Nrisimha appeared.

**18.** An ancient tale has come to My mind;
listen, all, to My words with one-pointed attention.

**19.** There lived here a cowherd [named] Punda;
Punda tilled the land from morning to evening[^46-6].

**20.** On the land he raised a crop called "shasa";
the mayambu crop turned out very abundant.

**21.** Day and night he guards the crop — he has no rest;
and no one knows when he [ever] goes home.

**22.** One day he reflected within himself:
'I will no longer come to guard the crop.'

**22+1.** Thinking it over, he decided thus: 'I will entrust [it] to Krishna;
if I entrust it to Him, I can turn to other work.'

**22+2.** Calling the name of Krishna, he entrusted the crop to Him:
'In Your name I will give something to the Vaishnavas'[^46-7].

**23.** So Punda lives, rejoicing in his heart —
and suddenly he sees: something is eating the crop.

**23+1.** Seeing this, the cowherd grieved and thought:
'Krishna, You have ruined my whole crop!'

**23+2.** Weeping, the cowherd said: 'Listen, Narayana!
Who has eaten my crop — I will see with my own eyes.'

**23+3.** Saying this, he took shelter in a hut
and stayed awake, guarding the crop, in great distress[^46-8].

**24.** The next night he keeps watch — and in the third watch
suddenly there came a huge boar.

**25.** Seeing it, the cowherd was on his guard:
the boar eats the crop, twitching its ears.

**26.** It eats the crop, tears the vines to its heart's content —
seeing this, the cowherd strung his bow:

**27.** 'Eat the crop, tear the vines, twitch your ears —
today at my hand you shall lose your life!'

**28.** Saying this, he took aim and loosed the arrow;
it struck deep — the boar utters the name of Rama[^46-9].

**29.** Running off, it hid inside a mountain cave;
seeing this, Punda the cowherd was bewildered:

**30.** 'It is a boar — why then does it repeat "Rama, Rama"?
This is no boar — this is Bhagavan Himself!'

**31.** Thinking so, Punda, pained at heart,
went up to the cave and calls out [for] an answer.

**32.** 'Who are You? Who are You?' he says, but gets no answer;
with an anguished heart he fasted three days:

**33.** 'What have I done, low wretch that I am!
There is no sinner like me — a vile unbeliever.'

**34.** Mercy arose in the Lord, the treasury of compassion;
with a voice from the sky He says: 'I am Bhagavan.

**35.** You wounded Me, and I ruined you —
do not worry, go to your home'[^46-10].

**36.** Hearing this, Punda was tormented still more:
'Fasting day after day, I will give up this body.'

**37.** So he fasted many days;
suddenly a voice arose in the sky:

**38.** 'Why, foolish Punda, do you die for no reason?
There is no fault [in you] — go to your home.'

**39.** Again Punda speaks in a voice full of pain:
'I shot an arrow at You — what use is my life?

**40.** Even if I die, this sin of mine will not be washed away;
for such a sin the punishment of Yama is my due.

**41.** By what atonement shall I be purified?
For it was I who shot an arrow at You!

**41+1.** On Your tender body I inflicted such pain —
shame, shame on my life: I wounded You!

**41+2.** My forefathers, Lord, have gone to hell,
and those who see me will go to hell'[^46-11].

**42.** Hearing this, the voice came again:
'There is no fault — I am boundlessly pleased.

**42+1.** All the offences you committed in former births —
all your sins I have taken on Myself at once.

**42+2.** Know: your body is My body, in every way;
truly, you have caused Me no pain'[^46-12].

**43.** Hearing this, Punda speaks with joined palms:
'By Your command I speak, setting fear aside:

**44.** how shall I know that this sin of mine is washed away?
If I receive grace and a witness, I shall be content.

**45.** I will tell this to the king —
give him too the same command.

**46.** Then I shall believe, having a witness for my heart,
and all people will know that You have made me happy.'

**47.** Then the Lord commanded again:
'As you have said, so it shall be: you have received the boon.'

**48.** Hearing this, Punda rejoiced
and in great haste came to the king's gate.

**49.** He said to the gatekeeper: 'Listen, best of gatekeepers!
Bring to the king's notice all that I shall say.

**50.** I shall tell of a wondrous thing, unknown to people;
hearing it, the king will love me.'

**51.** Hearing this, the gatekeeper told the king,
and at the king's command Punda came before [him].

**52.** Falling down like a staff, he tells everything in detail —
he related everything from beginning to end.

**53.** Hearing it, the maharaja was amazed
and said to Punda: 'Tell it for certain.'

**54.** Again Punda speaks with certainty:
'Come there, O king — dispel your amazement.

**55.** As the Thakura commanded me,
the same command you too will soon receive.'

**56.** The king says: 'If Ishvara commands,
for life I shall be your servant.'

**57.** Saying this, the king set out in haste,
on foot he went to where the mountain cave was.

**58.** At the mouth of the mountain cave, with all his heart,
he prays at length, rolling on the ground.

**59.** The Thakura relented — a command arose in the sky:
'Not false, O king, listen, are Punda's words.

**59+1.** You are the witness: Punda has become Mine;
over him Yama has power no more.'

**59+2.** Hearing this, the king dances with joy,
clasping the cowherd's feet, he weeps:

**59+3.** 'You have become my guru and united me with Krishna,
you have let me hear words from Krishna's own lips!'

**59+4.** Together with the queens [the king] falls at the cowherd's feet;
seeing this, Krishna was filled with mercy:

**59+5.** 'You did not judge My devotee by caste —
therefore, O king, I said that I would appear to you[^46-13].

**60.** Pour milk on this place:
by the pouring of milk you will see Me before your eyes.'

**61.** Hearing this, the king, with a joyful heart,
had it proclaimed through the kingdom that milk be brought.

**62.** By the Lord's command they pour milk there —
and suddenly they see before their eyes the crown of a head.

**63.** All kinds of instruments play, the joy is boundless;
[all] float in joy — in a shoreless ocean of happiness.

**64.** On every side resounds "Hari! Hari!" —
all the people dance with both arms raised.

**65.** As much milk as they pour — so far the body rises;
the body rises — the deep [hollow of the] navel is seen.

**66.** With joyful hearts they pour still more milk,
hoping to see all the limbs of the Lord.

**67.** The body rose — the knees are seen before their eyes.
'Pour no milk' — was the command: the measure is reached.

**68.** Yet with joyful hearts they pour much more milk —
but the soles of the feet did not rise in the end.

**69.** Then a command arose in the sky:
'The feet will not rise — strive no more'[^46-14].

**70.** Hearing this, the king [felt] both joy and sorrow;
having received grace, all hold a great festival.

**71.** He raised a temple and established various offerings;
[all] gaze and cannot gaze enough, their hearts full of love.

**71+1.** The king humbly said to Punda:
'Be king of this kingdom — show me mercy.'

**71+2.** The cowherd answers: 'You speak like an ignorant man:
I will not take the kingdom — why do you pain me?

**71+3.** You and I together will joyfully serve Krishna;
what happiness is there in a kingdom, O king, if one leaves Govinda?'

**71+4.** Hearing this, the king humbly said with joined palms:
'You and I — we shall both be entitled to the service'[^46-15].

**72.** So the king lives with a joyful heart;
suddenly a merchant came in a boat.

**72+1.** With him were two wives, most beautiful;
they want to go with him to see Sri Hari.

**72+2.** The merchant will not take them along — he is ashamed;
both wives weep, clasping the merchant's feet:

**72+3.** 'You are [our] guru — take us with you, show us Krishna,
do not, lord, destroy our good fortune.'

**72+4.** The merchant says: 'I will not take you with me;
I will bring prasada — stay at home.'

**72+5.** They say: 'What you have said — so be it,
[but] truly the desire to see Krishna has arisen in us.'

**72+6.** Then the merchant angrily says to them both:
'Go and see Krishna, and I will stay at home.'

**72+7.** Hearing this, both wives were sad at heart
and resolved: 'We will leave our husband and serve Krishna.'

**72+8.** The beautiful women went, leaving their husband;
Govinda was filled with mercy, seeing their devotion.

**72+9.** The Lord awakened mercy in the merchant's heart,
and the merchant, coming, sees his wives:

**72+10.** 'Shame, shame on me, worthless and sinful at heart!
It is not right to dishonour such wives.'

**72+11.** The merchant says: 'Come, I will take you with me:
you are utterly pure, your bodies are holy.

**72+12.** If a wife is devoted to Krishna, it is her husband's good fortune:
she is honoured by all, great is her worth'[^46-16].

**73.** To see the Thakura that merchant came,
with his two wives he went into the temple.

**74.** Bowing to the Lord, the merchant went outside;
the merchant went out — and the temple doors closed.

**75.** Turning back, he sees — the two wives are not beside him:
they are inside the temple, conversing with the Lord.

**76.** Understanding this, the merchant offers praises with laments;
the Thakura relented and showed him grace.

**77.** The temple doors opened — he sees: both
have turned to stone and attained the Lord's feet.

**77+1.** They went, leaving their husband, to attain Krishna as Husband —
therefore they attained Krishna as their steadfast Husband[^46-17].

**78.** Deeming this his good fortune, the merchant fell at [the Lord's] feet;
showing grace, the Lord says: 'Ask a boon.'

**79.** Falling at His feet, the merchant bows
and asks a boon: 'Let Your name be after my name.

**80.** My father and mother gave me the name "Jiyada"' —
so after his own name he asks as a boon a name for the Lord.

**81.** Therefore the name "Jiyada-Nrisimha" became renowned"[^46-18].
In joy this Lochana Dasa sings [His] glories.

*Raga sindhura*

**82.** Then Mahaprabhu, having seen Jiyada-Nrisimha
and spent that day there, set out on the next day.

**83.** He walks along the road, His heart in the power of prema;
the Lord arrived at the town of Kanchi[^46-19].

**84.** Kanchi-nagara is a town full of jewels;
seeing the town, the best of sannyasis was pleased.

**85.** Never does the Lord look on the faces of worldly men —
[and yet] the Lord suddenly came to the king's gate.

**85+1.** The king, having bathed in the Godavari with the brahmanas,
came to his inner apartments and joyfully serves Krishna.

**85+2.** At that time the Lord came to the gate —
His radiance supremely beautiful, the enchanter of Madana[^46-20].

**86.** Coming to the king's gate, He spoke to the doorkeeper
and quietly asked: "Where is the prince?"[^46-21]

**87.** Seeing the Lord, the doorkeeper bows
and says within himself: "This is Bhagavan."

**88.** The Lord says: "Give the prince a message:
it is for his sake that I have come here."

**89.** The doorkeeper went to where the prince was —
to his inner apartments, where he was worshipping the Deity.

**90.** Bowing, the doorkeeper gives the message:
"A gosani of great spirit has come to the gate."

**91.** Hearing this, the king said nothing,
and the doorkeeper in fear drew back.

**92.** Returning to the gate, the doorkeeper reports:
"I could not deliver Your message.

**93.** The king is worshipping the Deity in his inner apartments —
who has the power to go in there?"

**94.** Hearing this, the Lord smiles within Himself
and went Himself to where [the king] is worshipping.

**95.** With one portion of Himself He stays at the gate, with another He goes
to where Ramananda Raya is worshipping[^46-22].

**96.** [Ramananda] meditates on Krishna — and sees Gaurachandra;
again he meditates, chanting the great mantra —

**97.** and again he sees that same Gaura before his eyes.
"What is this? What is this?" he wonders within himself.

**98.** Again he meditates with a steadfast heart —
and again Gaurachandra enters his heart.

**99.** "What? What?" he says, opens his eyes and looks all around —
and suddenly sees Gaurachandra, the best of sannyasis[^46-23].

**100.** Seeing the sannyasi, the king rose in awe,
bowed at His feet and gazes at [Him] unhurriedly.

**101.** From head to foot he gazes at the Lord's body;
seeing the golden body, joy arose in his heart.

**102.** He was amazed: how did the sannyasi come in?
Smiling, he says to the Lord:

**103.** "How did You enter my apartments?
By great fortune I have seen Your feet."

**104.** The Lord says: "Why do you not know your own self?
You do not recognize Me — and I have come to take you."

**104+1.** Thus the Lord speaks in sweet words:
"You do not recognize Me — I am the son of Nanda."

**104+2.** Hearing this, the king, his eyes brimming with tears, [says]:
"Then show me that form — so my heart will have a witness"[^46-24].

**105.** Hearing this, the Lord laughed aloud;
the Lord makes Himself known and reveals Himself.

**106.** All that was there — dark, white, red —
everything appears as one golden form.

**107.** That colour of tested, purest gold
the Lord gave up — and became dark and glossy.

**108.** His body has the colour of the kanada flower,
a peacock feather on His head, a flute at His lips.

**109.** Various ornaments on His body, He is lustrous black,
dressed in yellow garments, a forest garland on His neck.

**110.** Seeing this, the maharaja rejoiced at heart,
and the Lord became golden once more[^46-25].

**111.** Beasts, birds, trees, and all the creepers and leaves
glitter there in the radiance of the golden body.

**112.** Seeing this, Ramananda Raya understood what it was:
overcome with prema, he clasped the feet of his Lord.

**112+1.** Again the Lord took a dark body —
thrice-bent, a flute at His lips, wearing yellow garments.

**112+2.** On His left is Radha, a supreme beauty, a great radiance;
all around are the gopis, young maidens of lovely form.

**112+3.** In Vrindavana, in a temple of jewels, on a throne,
the king sees [Him] — supreme bliss — with Radha.

**112+4.** Again the Lord took on His golden form —
red garments on His body, as on a great sage[^46-26].

**113.** Fallen at [His] feet, [Ramananda] weeps, his body powerless;
taking him by the hand, the Lord went outside [with him].

**113+1.** Such a manifestation the king beheld unexpectedly;
ten days the Lord stayed with the king.

**113+2.** Many were their talks about Krishna;
even Ananta does not know how to retell them in full.

**113+3.** Endless are the lilas of Chaitanya, beyond the reach of the Vedas;
some lila one devotee or another describes at length.

**113+4.** Who has the power to tell everything from beginning to end?
As one writes and writes, the book only grows[^46-27].

**114.** The meeting of Ramananda Raya and the Lord —
a song of the virtues of Gora; this Lochana Dasa sings it.

[^46-1]: Rasaraja ("king of rasa", Krishna) and Mahabhava (Radha) in one form. According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 8), at the end of the talks on the Godavari the Lord showed Ramananda just this form; in Lochana Dasa (vv. 105–110) the Lord shows the form of Krishna, and in the 1983 edition Krishna with Radha.
[^46-2]: On Narahari and Gadadhara see en/24, v. 1. In the 1983 edition the Shesha-khanda is divided into chapters; chapter 1 bears the same title as Bhaktisiddhanta's section: "The Lord's Journey to the South."
[^46-3]: Kurma-kshetra (Shrikurmam) is a shrine of Vishnu in the form of the Tortoise on the coast south of Orissa. Vv. 3–14 — cf. Murari 3.13–3.14.13–20 and the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 7): the brahmana Kurma receives the Lord at his home, and the leper brahmana Vasudeva is healed by His embrace. Lochana Dasa does not mention leprosy; "became pure" (v. 6) may hint at the healing.
[^46-4]: Cf. Murari 3.14.19 and the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 7.128): "Whomever you meet, instruct him about Krishna."
[^46-5]: Jiyada-Nrisimha is the Deity of Varaha-Nrisimha at Simhachalam, near Vishakhapatnam. The Lord tells the tale of this Deity (vv. 16–81) also in Murari (3.14.21–32), more briefly; in the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 8) the Lord only visits Him.
[^46-6]: "Punda" — in Murari (3.14.23) "Pundaya"; it may be not a name but the name of a caste. The words "shasa" (in the 1983 edition "shasha", apparently cucumbers) and "mayambu" (so too in Murari) are unclear; they are given as they read.
[^46-7]: Vv. 22+1 — 22+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. The 1983 edition reads the second line of v. 22: "To whom shall I entrust the guarding of the crop?"
[^46-8]: Vv. 23+1 — 23+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^46-9]: The boar is the Lord Himself in the form of Varaha; by the name of Rama which He utters the cowherd recognizes Bhagavan (v. 30). Cf. Murari 3.14.24–25.
[^46-10]: The sense of the first line is not quite clear; we understand: "you wounded Me, and I ate your crop — we are quits."
[^46-11]: Vv. 41+1 — 41+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^46-12]: Vv. 42+1 — 42+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them partly in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. "Your body is My body" are words of the Lord's love for His devotee (cf. Bhagavata 9.4.68: "The sadhus are My heart, and I am their heart"), not a doctrine of the identity of the soul and God.
[^46-13]: Vv. 59+1 — 59+5 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. The thought that a devotee is not judged by birth is constant in Lochana Dasa and in all Gaudiya Vaishnavas.
[^46-14]: Cf. Murari 3.14.26–28: at the Lord's word the place is sprinkled with milk, and He reveals His form. The body of the Deity rises out of the earth up to the knees; the feet remain hidden.
[^46-15]: Vv. 71+1 — 71+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^46-16]: Vv. 72+1 — 72+12 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Cf. the wives of the brahmanas (yajna-patnis) who went to Krishna in spite of their husbands' prohibition (Bhagavata 10.23): devotion to Krishna stands above worldly ties, and a righteous husband accepts it as his good fortune.
[^46-17]: V. 77+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives it only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. The tale of the wives who turned to stone at the feet of the Deity is a local legend; in Murari (3.14.29–30) the merchant comes with one wife, and nothing is said of her fate. Attaining Krishna as Husband (pati) is no worldly bond: Krishna is the true Lord and Beloved of every soul.
[^46-18]: So too in Murari (3.14.31–32): the merchant asks that the Lord bear his name — "for Hari is ever subject to His devotees."
[^46-19]: So in A and C and in Murari (3.15.1). The 1983 edition reads Vidyanagara (in v. 84 too), as in the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 8), where Ramananda is governor of a province on the Godavari under King Prataparudra, and the Lord meets him on the bank of the Godavari. Kanchi (Kanchipuram) lies much farther south; in Lochana Dasa the Lord only afterwards goes "by way of the Godavari" to Panchavati (v. 116).
[^46-20]: Vv. 85+1 — 85+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote (after v. 86), as an additional reading of one manuscript.
[^46-21]: Ramananda Raya, son of Bhavananda Raya, was a high nobleman of Orissa; Lochana Dasa calls him now "prince" (rajaputra), now "king".
[^46-22]: While remaining at the gate, the Lord at the same time appears to Ramananda in the inner apartments: He can be present in many places at once (cf. Krishna's presence in every palace of Dvaraka, Bhagavata 10.69). "Portion" (amsha) here is not a part cut off from the whole: the Lord is fully present in each of His manifestations.
[^46-23]: Vv. 96–99 — cf. Murari 3.15.2–3: having finished his worship of Krishna, Ramananda contemplates the son of the king of Vraja and three times sees the form of Gauranga, and on opening his eyes he sees Him before him.
[^46-24]: Vv. 104+1 — 104+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript and of a printed edition. In Murari (3.15.4) the Lord says: "How is it that you do not remember, O bee at the lotus feet of Sri Radhika?"
[^46-25]: The 1903 edition gives vv. 107–110 only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript and of a printed edition. The kanada flower is apparently a dark-blue flower (commelina) to which Krishna's colour is compared. According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 8), Ramananda first sees the Lord as a dark cowherd boy, and then the Lord reveals to him the form of Rasaraja and Mahabhava — Krishna and Radha in one body.
[^46-26]: Vv. 112+1 — 112+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript (with one more couplet about the weeping queens). The vision of Radha and Krishna on a throne in the jewelled temple of Vrindavana is a vision of the eternal, transcendental lila of the Divine Couple. Red garments are the garments of a sannyasi.
[^46-27]: Vv. 113+1 — 113+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; they are absent from the 1903 edition. In the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 8) the Lord likewise spends several days and nights with Ramananda in talks about Krishna, and Krishnadasa Kaviraja relates these talks in detail.

---

# Shesha-khanda, part 2 (portion 47)

*The end of the section "The Lord's Journey to the South": Panchavati; Ranganatha and Trimalla Bhatta; the meeting with Paramananda Puri and the prophecy of Madhavendra Puri. The section "The Lord Sees Vrindavana": the deliverance of the seven palms, Setubandha, the return to Puri; Nrisimhananda paves a road in his mind; the journey to Mathura through Kashi and Prayaga, the meeting with Rupa and Sanatana; the brahmana Krishnadasa and his account of the twelve forests*

*Raga shri; disha: "He takes away sins, torments and the fear of Yama. Glory to the son of Shachi, glory, glory!"*

**115.** Then Mahaprabhu, in that joyful play,
walks on — and His body in bliss was filled with thrill.

**116.** Thus, step by step, He walks along the road
and by way of the Godavari enters Panchavati[^47-1].

**117.** This great holy place named Panchavati —
there Sita, Lakshmana and Sri Rama lived.

**118.** Seeing Panchavati, the Lord lost consciousness in prema
and again and again calls: "Sri Rama! Lakshmana!"

**119.** "Here Lakshmana built a hut;
from here Rama went to kill the deer.

**120.** After Sri Rama went Lakshmana —
and here Ravana carried off Sita."

**121.** Saying this, the Lord weeps, overcome with prema;
now He cries: "Strike! Strike!", now He cries: "Seize him! Seize him!"

**122.** Loudly He calls: "Lakshmana! Lakshmana!" —
and remembering Sita, weeps with a fainting heart[^47-2].

**123.** The companions who were with Him cannot console Him;
Mahaprabhu Himself regains His composure.

**124.** Then, on another day, the Thakura went along the road
and little by little reached the bank of the Kaveri.

**125.** On the bank of the Kaveri He sees Sri Ranganatha;
seeing Him, He dances in prema with His own people.

**126.** There Trimalla Bhatta, seeing the Thakura,
gazes in amazement at the golden body.

**127.** The radiance of His body and the surges of prema!
His hairs standing on end in thrill outdo the filaments of the kadamba.

**128.** His body towers over all people like Sumeru —
a wish-fulfilling tree laden with the fruits and flowers of prema.

**129.** "Hari! Hari!" He calls in a loud voice;
seeing this, all the people who crowd round on every side weep.

**130.** Seeing such things, that Trimalla Bhattacharya
in wonder grasped the whole marvellous matter:

**131.** "This is Bhagavan Himself — no one else!"
He knew for certain: this is the life of all beings.

**132.** Knowing this, Trimalla Bhatta Raya
takes the Lord to his own dwelling[^47-3].

**132+1.** The Lord came to his house at the beginning of [the month of] Ashadha;
in all beings devotion to Krishna grows from day to day.

**132+2.** There He saw the Ratha-yatra,
and Sri Shachinandana danced before the chariot.

**132+3.** Staying [there] in Shravana, the Lord celebrated the swing festival;
all dance in the sankirtana of [the Lord's] names and qualities.

**132+4.** Staying in Bhadra, He celebrated the festival of Krishna's birth:
much did Gorachand dance in the dress of a cowherd.

**132+5.** Staying in Ashvina, the Lord, the son of Shachi,
performs sankirtana of the Name with the devotees[^47-4].

**133.** Conquered by [Trimalla's] love, Mahaprabhu came under his sway
and, filled with the highest joy, spent the chaturmasya [with him].

**134.** Having spent the chaturmasya, the Lord quickly set out [again]
and on the way met Paramananda Puri[^47-5].

**135.** Seeing each other, both rejoiced;
they gaze at each other, and tears flow from the eyes of both.

**136.** Looking [at the Lord], Paramananda Puri remembered
what his guru Madhavendra Puri had said:

**137.** "In the first twilight of Kali the Lord of Lakshmi will appear —
a sannyasi with a golden body, dwelling near the Daru-brahman"[^47-6].

**138.** "To establish in the Kali-yuga the dharma of sankirtana
Krishna will be born in the first twilight [of this yuga].

**139.** Golden, tall of body, arms reaching to the knees,
a neck like a lion's, shoulders like an elephant's, lotus eyes.

**140.** The Lord, an ocean of compassion, the abode of prema,
out of His own mercy will show grace.

**141.** I have not the fortune to see Him with my own eyes;
when you see Him, remember me."

**142.** These words of his guru came to his mind,
and he knew for certain: this is Bhagavan Himself.

**142+1.** "Madhavendra!" he repeated, remembering [his guru],
and wept with joy in his heart.

**142+2.** Hearing him glorify Madhavendra, the Lord dances,
and the devotees, crying "Hari! Hari!", dance close by.

**142+3.** Now and again He gives loud shouts in supreme bliss;
"Madhavendra!" cries the Lord and weeps in the bliss of prema:

**142+4.** "Today My sannyasa has borne fruit:
the name of Madhavendra has entered My ears"[^47-7].

**143.** Seeing [this], Paramananda Puri bows;
"What are you doing?" says the Lord and lifts him up by the hands.

**144.** In great joy He embraced him tightly,
and the Thakura went on, says this Lochana Dasa.

## The Lord Sees Vrindavana

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* On the way to Setubandha Sriman Mahaprabhu showed the lila of delivering the seven palms. The ancient story of the seven palms [is this]: seven gandharvas, cursed by a sage, became trees; now, by the touch of the Lord, they attained liberation. Arriving at Setubandha, He, absorbed in prema, chanted the names of Rama, Lakshmana, Sita, Hanuman and others; then, having spent the chaturmasya on the bank of the Godavari, He returned again to Utkala, to Alalanatha. There He accepted as His own a devotee named Vishnudasa and, coming [to Purushottama], He stayed there and [spent] several months with the devotees in the bliss of kirtana. After this, setting out to see the Mathura-mandala, He met Rupa and Sanatana. Coming to Mathura, He, together with a devotee named Krishnadasa, absorbed in prema, saw on the eastern and western banks of the Yamuna the twelve forests, the prison of Devaki and Vasudeva, the houses of Kamsa, Ugrasena and others — countless places of Krishna's lilas[^47-8].

*Raga dhanashi; disha: "Gorachand is my life, Gora is my breath!"*

**1.** Hear attentively another wondrous story —
of the deliverance of the seven palms, as [the Lord] went along the road.

**2.** Those seven palms stand on that road;
seeing them, the Lord suddenly began to laugh.

**3.** Running up, He touched the seven palms —
and in the sky rose cries: "Glory! Glory! Glory!"

**4.** By a sage's curse seven gandharvas had dwelt [in them];
by the Lord's touch they attained deliverance.

**4+1.** Joining their palms, they prostrated themselves [before Him]
and, gaining divine bodies, all departed for Vaikuntha.

**4+2.** Seeing this, all the people bow;
all say: "This sannyasi is the avatara Rama"[^47-9].

**5.** Then Mahaprabhu goes along the road;
intoxicated with bliss, the Lord sings the glories of Hari.

**6.** In the bliss of prema He does not feel the weariness of the road
and little by little reached Setubandha.

**7.** Coming to Setubandha, He sees the linga of Rameshvara;
the Lord dances in bliss like a maddened lion.

**8.** Walking round the linga, He bows;
looking at Setubandha, He cries "Hari!" again and again.

**9.** In loving longing He weeps and calls: "Sri Rama! Lakshmana!";
at times, in His bhava, He calls: "Angada! Hanuman!"

**10.** Now in His bhava He calls: "Sugriva, My friend!" —
now in a frenzy He calls: "Vibhishana!"

**11.** Overcome with prema, He does not know one direction from another,
and looking at Setubandha He dances with all the devotees[^47-10].

**12.** Thus day and night He forgets Himself;
[at last] Mahaprabhu's mercy grew — to turn back.

**13.** So Mahaprabhu, going along the road [back],
again spent a chaturmasya, staying at the holy Godavari[^47-11].

**14.** Again the Thakura came to the land of the Udras;
in the bhava [of meeting] Jagannatha His prema grew beyond measure.

**15.** Then the Lord saw Sri Alalanatha
and made Vishnudasa the Oriya His own[^47-12].

**16.** Seeing Jagannatha, the Lord was filled with joyful excitement:
again and again raising His arms, He cries "Hari! Hari!"

**17.** Coming to Purushottama, the Lord stays in great happiness;
Lochana tells it — a great joy it is to people.

*Raga varadi; the tune "dhulakhela"*

**18.** Now I shall tell a story — hear the song of the virtues of Gora,
peerless in the three worlds.
In his mind he built an embankment, strewing it with pearls and coral —
a sannyasi named Nrisimhananda.

**19.** With gold, gems and rubies, with divine jewels on every side,
in his mind he built a road.
"Taking it as far as Mathura, I will offer it to Krishna" —
but just then his hour drew near.

**20.** The road was not finished, and grief remained in his heart;
he lamented within himself.
As far as Kanai's Natashala the road reached, and there it ended —
and the sannyasi attained Vaikuntha[^47-13].

**21.** This had been in his heart. The Lord suddenly set out —
no one knows where He is going.
Going on step by step, from Kanai's Natashala
Gora Raya turned back again.

**22.** The matter was not understood. Paramananda Puri says:
"Tell us, Lord, the reason for this."
Everything from beginning to end He then told him:
the reason was the fulfilment of a wish of the mind.

**23.** "From Purushottama all the way to Mathura
I will pave an embankment with gold, gems and rubies" —
such was the sannyasi's heart: "Along this road of mine
Gora, who wears the forest garland, will go."

**24.** Hear, all people, attentively, setting your minds on it,
of the manifestation of Sri Gorachand:
the wish of Nrisimhananda's mind Gaurachandra fulfilled —
of His glories this Lochana Dasa sings[^47-14].

*Raga shri; disha: "Gorachand — oh! — plays in Nilachala"*

**25.** And so in Nilachala the Lord with the devotees
delights in kirtana and stays in various sports.

**26.** Many devotees gathered there;
the Lord enjoys prema — He dances and makes others dance.

**27.** All the devotees who lived in various lands
little by little came to the feet of Chaitanya.

**28.** In bliss the Lord dwells in Nilachala;
of all this I shall tell later in detail.

**29.** Suddenly [the Lord] has a wish in His mind: "I will go to Mathura";
longing grew in His heart, His mind as though maddened.

**30.** Chaitanya Thakura set out on the road to Mathura;
on the way the bliss of prema grew beyond measure.

**31.** In loving longing the Lord runs, both eyes red;
He runs with a lion's gait — no sooner seen than gone.

**32.** The companions who were with Him cannot keep up;
the Lord goes far ahead, calling [for Krishna].

**33.** By the road through Jharikhanda the Lord went in haste;
He made beasts, birds, trees, even stones weep.

**34.** Surrounding Gauranga, deer and tigers dance;
there is no violence [in them] — all dance joyfully near the Lord.

**35.** Having blessed all the forest animals,
Gauranga went on His way, delighting in prema[^47-15].

**36.** Little by little He came to holy Varanasi;
many great sannyasis live there.

**37.** Bowing to Vishveshvara, He goes on along the road;
at Prayaga, seeing Madhava, He rejoiced at heart.

**38.** Rupa and Sanatana Gosani met the Lord;
showing them grace, He imparted His power to them[^47-16].

**39.** There, bathing at the Veni and seeing the Akshaya-vata,
He crossed the Yamuna near Agra.

**40.** He saw a wondrous village named Renuka —
the place where Parashurama appeared.

**41.** There the Yamuna, turning [back], faces Vrindavana;
seeing this, the Lord, beside Himself, is happy with the happiness of prema[^47-17].

**42.** Coming to Rajagrama, He sees Gokula on the far bank;
unable to restrain His heart, He became distraught.

**43.** With great effort the Lord restrained His heart;
overwhelmed with bliss, He sees Mahavana on the far bank.

**44.** He went on and on some distance more —
and, drawing near, He sees Madhupura.

**45.** Seeing Madhupura, the Lord became mad at heart;
overcome with prema, He seemed to lose consciousness.

**46.** Crying "Akrura! Akrura!", He fell to the ground;
in the bhava of separation, [when Krishna left for] Mathura, He fainted[^47-18].

**47.** Not knowing day or night, He remains in that place;
for three days the Lord was without consciousness.

**48.** People come and go and look at this marvel.
There lived [there] a foremost brahmana named Krishnadasa.

**49.** Seeing the Lord, he thinks within himself:
"From where has this jewel among men come?

**50.** By great fortune I have seen His feet;
is this Shuka or Prahlada? — so my mind takes it."

**51.** The Lord, overcome with prema, asked him:
"What is your name? Tell Me — listen, best of brahmanas."

**52.** The brahmana says: "Listen, listen, best of sannyasis:
my name is Krishnadasa" — so he answered.

**53.** Hearing this, the Lord laughed aloud:
"You, Krishnadasa, know everything of Krishna.

**54.** Your words have refreshed My body;
you will show Me all that is anywhere, in every detail.

**55.** This Mathura-mandala is Krishna's inner abode;
you, an experienced devotee, know everything.

**56.** You know what Krishna did and where —
show Me the Mathura-mandala, place by place."

**57.** The brahmana says: "I do not know all the places;
I know only the places of the twelve forests."

**58.** Hearing this, the Lord laughs in the bliss of prema
and manifested [His] power in his body.

**59.** In great bliss [the brahmana] says: "I will show everything;
from Krishna's birth to the slaying of Kamsa — I will tell it all."

**60.** The brahmana says: "Listen, listen, listen, sir:
I know for certain — You are the son of Nanda.

**61.** Seeing You, I have seen Vraja:
suddenly everything came back to my memory.

**62.** I [will reveal] the inner meaning of every place I know —
where Bhagavan was born and what He did."

**63.** Hearing this, the Lord, with joy in His heart,
embraced Krishnadasa and sings the glories of Krishna[^47-19].

**64.** That day He spent in Krishnadasa's house;
all night [Krishnadasa] tells of the Mathura-mandala:

**65.** "In the midst of the Mathura-mandala is the blessed Yamuna,
on both banks of which Krishna sports in love.

**66.** On the eastern bank of the Yamuna there are five forests,
on the western seven forests; now I shall tell of them.

**67.** In these twelve forests are Krishna's pastimes;
apart from devotees no one knows their secret.

**68.** Here is Kamsa's palace — west of the Yamuna;
to the north of it is the forest named Vrindavana.

**69.** From Mathura to it is one yojana of road;
of it I shall tell many secret things.

**70.** To the south-west of it is a forest named Kumuda,
one and a quarter yojanas' journey from Mathura.

**71.** South of it, Lord, is the Khadira-vana,
a yojana and a half's journey from Mathura.

**72.** The Tala-vana, Lord, is south of Mathura:
from Mathura it is half a yojana away.

**73.** There is a stream — a river named Manasa-Ganga:
west of Vrindavana, north-east of Mathura.

**74.** From the Kamyaka-vana [it flows] towards Madhuvana
and west of the Kaliya-daha enters the Yamuna.

**75.** There is also a stream named Sarasvati there:
north of Mathura it enters the Yamuna.

**76.** West of Mathura is the hill of Govardhana,
three and a half yojanas, counting from Mathura.

**77.** I shall tell of the Kamyaka-vana — west of Govardhana:
from Mathura, as people reckon, three and a half yojanas.

**78.** The forest named Bahula is north-east of Mathura,
beyond the Manasa-Ganga, at two yojanas.

**79.** These are the seven forests west of the Yamuna;
now I shall tell of the five forests on the eastern bank.

**80.** The forest named Mahavana is near the Yamuna,
one yojana's journey from Mathura.

**81.** To the west of it is the forest named Bilva,
half a yojana from Mathura, on the far bank.

**82.** To the north of it is the forest named Loha;
to the north-east of it is the forest named Bhandira.
