**83.** These two forests lie together on the bank of the Yamuna;
from Mahavana, people say, three and a half yojanas.

**84.** These are the twelve forests — the Mathura-mandala;
all the places of Krishna's pastimes I will show [You]"[^47-20].

[^47-1]: Panchavati is the forest on the Godavari (near present-day Nasik) where Rama, Sita and Lakshmana lived in exile; cf. Murari 3.15.6.
[^47-2]: The Lord, in the mood of Rama, lives through the abduction of Sita as though it were happening now. Cf. Murari 3.16.3–4 (on the way to Setubandha).
[^47-3]: Shrirangam on the Kaveri; cf. Murari 3.15.7–17. In the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 9) the Lord spends the chaturmasya in the house of Venkata Bhatta, Trimalla's brother.
[^47-4]: Vv. 132+1 — 132+5 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of a printed edition. The chaturmasya is the four months of the rains (Ashadha to Ashvina), when sannyasis do not travel.
[^47-5]: Vv. 134–144 — cf. Murari 3.15.19–25. In the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 9) the Lord meets Paramananda Puri at the Rishabha hill after Shrirangam.
[^47-6]: In the 1983 edition the verse is cited as from the Vayu Purana (in A the heading of the quotation is corrupted by the OCR); the 1903 edition gives it only in a footnote, as an additional reading of a printed edition. Bhaktisiddhanta's prose rendering of the verse is not translated. "The first twilight of Kali" is the opening period of the Kali-yuga; on the Daru-brahman (Jagannatha) see en/45, note 15. Vv. 138–141 are Madhavendra Puri's words, a Bengali paraphrase of this verse.
[^47-7]: Vv. 142+1 — 142+4 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of one manuscript. Madhavendra Puri is the guru of Ishvara Puri, and so the Lord's "grandfather" in the line of gurus.
[^47-8]: The summary in A is badly corrupted by the OCR in places; gaps are filled from the sense and from Bhaktisiddhanta's table of contents (chapter 2 of the Shesha-khanda). In the 1983 edition chapter 2 ("The Lord Sees Vrindavana") begins later — at v. 18 (the song of Nrisimhananda); vv. 1–17 there close chapter 1.
[^47-9]: Vv. 4+1 — 4+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them only in a footnote, as an additional reading of a printed edition. Cf. Murari 3.16.1–2 (in Murari, tamala trees). According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 9), the Lord embraced the seven palms and sent them to Vaikuntha; these are the seven palms that Rama pierced with one arrow to convince Sugriva of His strength.
[^47-10]: Cf. Murari 3.16.3–6. The Lord, in the mood of Rama, calls His associates — Lakshmana, Angada, Hanuman, Sugriva, Vibhishana. Rameshvara is the linga of Shiva installed, by tradition, by Rama Himself; for Vaishnavas Shiva is the greatest of devotees.
[^47-11]: A second chaturmasya (on the Godavari) is peculiar to Lochana Dasa's account; according to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 9), in the South the Lord spent one chaturmasya — at Shrirangam — and on the way back only met Ramananda again on the Godavari (cf. Murari 3.16.9–11).
[^47-12]: Alalanatha is Vishnu at Brahmagiri, south of Puri. Vishnudasa is in Murari (3.16.12) the brahmana with whom the Lord sees Alalanatha.
[^47-13]: "Attained Vaikuntha" — left this world. Nrisimhananda (Pradyumna Brahmachari) in Murari (3.17.5–8) and in the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 1) paves in his mind a road for the Lord from Bengal (from Kuliya) to Vrindavana, but cannot take it beyond Kanai's Natashala and therefore foretells that the Lord will turn back from there; in those accounts he does not die and himself tells the devotees about it. Kanai's Natashala is a village on the Ganga near Rajmahal, where the Lord, on His first attempt to go to Vrindavana, turned back.
[^47-14]: In Lochana Dasa the Lord turns back from Kanai's Natashala even before the journey to Mathura told next; of His route through Bengal (Ramakeli, Kuliya) Lochana Dasa says nothing here.
[^47-15]: Cf. the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 17): going through the forests of Jharikhanda, the Lord makes tigers and deer dance together and chant the name of Krishna. In Murari (4.01) the Lord goes from Puri through Kashi.
[^47-16]: Vv. 36–38 — cf. Murari 4.01–4.02.1. According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 19–20), the Lord meets Rupa at Prayaga and Sanatana at Kashi, on the way back from Vrindavana; Lochana Dasa places the meeting with both on the way to Mathura. "Imparted His power" (shakti-sanchara) — empowered them to reveal to the world the teaching and the lilas of Vraja.
[^47-17]: Vv. 39–45 — cf. Murari 4.02.2–6. The Akshaya-vata is the "imperishable banyan" at Prayaga; the Veni (Triveni) is the confluence of the Ganga, Yamuna and Sarasvati. V. 41 we understand as in Murari (4.02.5): the Yamuna "ever turned towards the forest of Vrinda."
[^47-18]: Akrura took Krishna away from Vraja to Mathura, and the gopis in separation blamed him. Seeing Mathura, the Lord lives through the separation of the gopis (of Radha) from Krishna.
[^47-19]: Vv. 48–63 — cf. Murari 4.02.9–13. The brahmana Krishnadasa, by the Lord's word and power, becomes His guide through the Mathura-mandala.
[^47-20]: Vv. 65–84 — cf. Murari 4.03.3–20 (the account of the twelve forests). Distances: a yojana is about 12–13 km. "Three and a half" (vv. 76, 77, 83) is the 1903 edition's reading ("আউট"); A and B read "আট" ("eight"), apparently a corruption of the same word. Only eleven forests are named; the twelfth in Murari is Bhadra.

---

# Shesha-khanda, part 3 (portion 48)

*The section "The Lord Sees Vrindavana" (continued): Krishnadasa shows the Lord Mathura — the walls, Kamsa's palace, the prison, the "place where He passed water" as an infant, Uddhava's house, the twelve ghats, Kamsa's well; the story of the Setubandha lake; the places of Krishna's infant and childhood lilas in Mahavana and Gokula; Nanda entrusts the Child to Radha in Bilvavana; the cowherds move to Vrindavana; the places of Krishna's sports with the cowherd boys — the Lord in the mood of a friend*

**85.** Thus in conversation the morning came;
the Lord performed His morning rites as prescribed.

**86.** With a longing heart He called Krishnadasa;
His love was more than His body could hold.

**87.** The Lord set out to see the Mathura-mandala;
Ishvara Himself plays a prank on Krishnadasa[^48-1].

**88.** Krishnadasa says: "Lord, turn Your mind here:
see, on three sides of the city — the fortifications.

**89.** On the east the river Yamuna flows southward;
on two sides of the fortifications are the north and south gates.

**90.** See here Kamsa's palace — south-west of the city;
it has two gates, to the east and to the north.

**91.** See — north of the palace a platform for sitting;
and here, see, in the north-west of the city — the prison.

**92.** And here, see, south of it — the 'place where He passed water';
I will tell about it in detail — listen attentively.

**93.** In fear of Kamsa, Vasudeva carries away his son;
suddenly Krishna passed water in his arms.

**94.** Here Vasudeva quickly sat down;
Krishna passed water — and the stone melted.

**95.** The mark of the water remained on this stone;
therefore people call this place the 'place where He passed water'[^48-2].

**96.** North of here, see, is Uddhava's house."
Hearing this, the Lord's tears ran in two streams down His throat.

**97.** His whole body from head to foot thrilled:
each hair stood up like a kadamba filament.

**98.** "Here is Uddhava's house — I have come here now;
what Krishna did here I tell from My own experience.

**99.** Here took place the talk of Krishna and Uddhava;
I feel as if I saw it — and pain pierces My heart"[^48-3].

**100.** Saying this, the Lord looks all around;
then, with love, Krishnadasa speaks:

**101.** "East of Uddhava's [house], see, is the washerman's house;
and east of it, see, the dwelling of the garland-maker.

**102.** South of here, see, is Kubja's house;
south of it — the lovely arena.

**103.** See — south-east of it is Vasudeva's house."
Hearing this, the Lord smiles within Himself,

**104.** His voice falters, His face flushes a little.
"See — north-east of it is Ugrasena's house.

**105.** See — south of it is the Vishranti-ghata;
here the Deity named Gatashrama is renowned[^48-4].

**106.** When Krishna killed Kamsa and dragged him away, a channel was formed —
therefore south of here is the Kamsa-khali ghata.

**107.** See — south of it is the Prayaga-ghata;
south of it — the ghata named Tinduka.

**108.** South of here is the ghata called Saptatirtha;
south of it, see, [the ghata] named Rishitirtha.

**109.** South of here, see, is also Mokshatirtha;
south of it — the renowned Kotitirtha.

**110.** South of it, see, [the ghata] named Bodhitirtha;
to the south — Ganeshatirtha, there it is, see.

**111.** These are the twelve ghatas — the essence of all holy places.
And south of the city, see, there is also the arena.

**112.** South of it, see, is one more marvel:
the evil-minded King Kamsa dug a well.

**113.** 'Having killed Krishna, I will throw Him in here' — such was his plan;
Kamsa dug the well — [hence] the name Kamsa-kupa.

**114.** See — south-west of it is the Agastya-kunda;
north of here — the Setubandha lake."

**115.** Hearing this, the Lord cries: "What? What?" —
and His body was covered all over with thrill.

**116.** "Hear the account of the Setubandha lake;
listen attentively, Lord, with a collected mind.

**117.** Once Krishna, in the company of the gopis,
was playing the rasa dance on the bank of this lake.

**118.** He said to Radha: 'I am that very Raghunatha:
I killed Ravana together with the monkeys.'

**119.** Hearing this, Radha smiled faintly:
'Krishna is telling an untruth — that is what is on [His] mind.'

**120.** Seeing this, He, perturbed, asks Radha:
'Why do you laugh, Rai? Tell Me!'

**121.** Radha says: 'Tell no more untruths!
How could You have been the avatara Rama?

**122.** He is a great master of the senses, the Supreme Ishvara;
His conduct is not possible for You.

**123.** He dammed the ocean with trees and stones —
let us see You dam this lake!'

**124.** Hearing this, [Krishna] smiles gently:
'If I place bricks and stones in the water, they will float.'

**125.** Hearing this, the gopis say:
'We will bring stones — let us see You dam it now!

**126.** Do not boast in vain, listen, Kanai:
that stones float in water — we have never heard.'

**127.** The Thakura says: 'Bring these trees and stones —
I will dam this lake with stones.'

**128.** Hearing this, they carry bricks,
bring pieces of wood and whole stones.

**128+1.** Standing on one bank, Krishna dams the lake:
from this bank to that, stones were laid everywhere[^48-5].

**129.** With these trees and stones the lake was dammed;
'Well done, well done!' say the gopis, and Radha smiles faintly.

**130.** For Radha's sake a dam arose on the lake —
that is why it is called the Setubandha lake"[^48-6].

**131.** Hearing this, the Lord rejoiced at heart;
joyfully this Lochana Dasa sings the glories of Gora.

*Raga pathamanjari*

**132.** "North of here is the Saptasamudraka-kunda:
[here] Devaki's seven sons [were dashed] against a stone.

**133.** North of here, see, is the linga of Bhuteshvara;
see the Sarasvati-kunda to the north of the city.

**134.** Here, see, is the Dashashvamedha-ghata;
south of here — the way to the Soma-tirtha.

**135.** South of here is the Kanthabharana-majjana;
the stream of the Naga-tirtha flows, going down to Patala"[^48-7].

**136.** Then He went to Samyamana and the other kundas and ghatas
and walked round the city, absorbed in His own feelings.

**137.** Thus, wandering, the day passed;
having taken alms, the Lord spent the night.

**138.** Tormented by longing, the night seemed long to Him;
"Has it dawned? Has it dawned?" He asks, His heart aching.

**139.** Night turned to morning — His heart rejoices;
having performed the morning rites, He says: "Come, Krishnadasa!"

**140.** Krishnadasa says: "Lord, hear my word:
the land of the Mathura-mandala is twenty-one yojanas;

**141.** the twelve forests lie within six yojanas.
What Krishna did and where — I will show [You] everything.

**142.** Here Kamsa heard the words of Narada,
here he kept Vasudeva and Devaki.

**143.** Here Krishna appeared; seeing Him four-armed,
Vasudeva and Devaki here beg [Him to hide that form].

**144.** From here Vasudeva [went], taking Krishna in his arms,
while the guards, overcome with sleep, lay senseless.

**145.** Vasuki, spreading his hoods as an umbrella, hurries behind;
at the crossing of the Yamuna a she-jackal runs ahead.

**146.** Here is Mahavana, the dwelling of Nanda Ghosha;
here in her sleep the virtuous Yashoda bore a daughter.

**147.** Leaving the son in Nanda's house, [Vasudeva] took the daughter,
and calling [her] Devaki's daughter they deceived Kamsa.

**148.** When the wicked King Kamsa tried to kill the girl,
she went up into the sky as lightning.

**149.** Kamsa, having done wrong, praises both [Vasudeva and Devaki];
at that time he hears a voice from the sky.

**150.** Hearing that voice, he began to persecute the righteous:
he knew for certain that death [awaited him].

**151.** Having celebrated the festival for his son, Nanda came to Mathura;
Vasudeva said: 'Guard the child, keep Him hidden.'

**152.** Seven days old, Krishna killed Putana;
at a month old He overturned and smashed the cart.

**153.** Krishna kills Trinavarta, becoming heavy as the Bearer of the universe;
yawning, He showed His mother the universe in His belly.

**154.** After six months came the name-giving;
when He ate earth, He showed the universal form.

**155.** Here He danced, holding on to the churning rod;
here Yashoda went off when the milk boiled over.

**156.** Climbing on a mortar and piercing the pot in its sling,
Hari, His face turned upward, ate butter.

**157.** Here Krishna stole and ate butter;
here mother Yashoda bound Him to the mortar.

**158.** Here He broke the twin arjuna trees;
here, giving grain, the God Narayana ate fruit[^48-8].

**159.** South of Mahavana, see, is the town of Gokula:
here Damodara with the boys herded the calves.

**160.** Here, see, is the lovely Deity of Gopeshvara;
see how beautiful the Saptasamudraka-kunda is.

**161.** See — west of the village is Ayana's house;
south of it — the house of the cowherd Sundara.

**162.** In the middle of the village — here is Upananda's house;
to the west, see, is Ravana's forest of austerity.

**163.** See — north of here is the ashrama of Durvasa;
near by, see, is the lovely Lohavana[^48-9].

**164.** I will tell a marvel: here is Bilvavana —
here Nanda was, with Krishna in his arms.

**165.** Seeing Radha, Nanda said to Her:
'Take Krishna in your arms, carry Him home.'

**166.** At Nanda's word Radha took Krishna in Her arms;
[and He] kisses Her, as if in childish play.

**167.** Not understanding the matter, Radha carries Him along the road;
in a tight embrace He leaves the marks of His nails on Her breast.

**168.** Seeing such conduct, Radha was amazed;
prema arose in Her heart, but She did not show it[^48-10].

**169.** Here, see, is another deed of Krishna:
all the boys, tormented by thirst, are dying —

**170.** and He dug a pond with a cowherd's stick — there it is, see."
As soon as He heard this, Gaurachandra lost outer consciousness.

**171.** After some time Gaurachandra came back to Himself;
the Lord says: "Krishnadasa, what happened next?"

**172.** "Here, see, Upananda and all the others
held counsel, and all the cowherds agreed:

**173.** 'Unbearable is this oppression of the king, every day is danger';
at daybreak they all made ready the carts.

**174.** Seating the gopis in the carts, the cowherds
[set out] to settle near by, in Vrindavana.

**175.** With cries of 'hey, hey!' they go, driving the cows,
wraps on their feet, sticks in their hands, turbans on their heads.

**175+1.** Krishna and Balarama ride on a cart;
looking at Their faces, the cowherds go happily[^48-11].

**176.** Two months they lived in the Bhadra and Bhandira forests"[^48-12].
Joyfully this Lochana Dasa sings [His] glories.

**177.** "Then they crossed over nearer, to Vrindavana;
here they set the carts in a half-moon.

**178.** At the root of a kapittha tree He killed Vatsasura:
seizing him by the tail and legs, He lifted him and dashed him to the ground.

**179.** Here Bakasura swallowed Krishna and spat Him out;
seizing him by both beaks, He tore him apart and took his life.

**180.** In this pasture He plays with all the boys —
horns, flutes, sticks in hand, in various sports.

**181.** Some imitate the cry of some animal,
some want to catch the shadow of a flying bird."

**182.** Hearing this, Gaura, distraught at heart,
runs here and there like a boy.

**183.** He cries like a peacock, spreads [an imagined] tail;
His body is full of thrill, His eyes red.

**184.** "Brother, brother!" He calls, shouts: "Hey! Hey!";
"Shridama! Sudama!" He cries and embraces a tree.

**185.** Overwhelmed by the bhava of friendship, Gaura Raya,
maddened with prema, runs in all directions.

**186.** "Kali! Dhavali!" He calls again and again. —
"Where has Dhenukasura gone? I will kill him now!"

**187.** Saying this, He weeps; His body knows nothing outward.
Krishnadasa says: "This is He — the hero of the Yadus!"

**188.** And the companions with Him are in the same state:
they gaze at Gora's face, aware of nothing[^48-13].

**189.** After some time Gaurachandra came back to Himself
and again says to Krishnadasa: "Tell Me what happened."

**190.** "The serpent named Aghasura is Baka's younger brother;
here Krishna took his life.

**191.** Here the Yamuna used to be — now it is not;
here Brahma stole the calves and the boys.

**192.** For a year he kept them in [a cave of] Govardhana;
seeing those same calves and boys, Brahma offered praise.

**193.** [Here] Balarama killed Dhenuka and ate the palm fruits.
See — here on the Yamuna is the place Kaliya-daha.

**194.** Here He climbed the kadamba tree
and, leaping [into the water], subdued the serpent Kaliya.

**195.** Chilled through, Krishna came up on this ghata,
and then twelve suns rose in the sky.

**196.** Therefore people call it the ghata of the Twelve Adityas;
see before you the Deity of Kaliya-damana [the Subduer of Kaliya].

**197.** Here the boys and calves were burning in the forest fire;
drinking up the fire, He saved them all.

**198.** Here Krishna rode on Shridama's shoulders,
and Pralamba, having lost, carried Balarama on his shoulders.

**199.** The asura's illusion was revealed to Balarama:
He struck him on the head with His fist, and he gave up his life.

**200.** In the Bhandira forest — the death of Aghasura[^48-14].
Near by, see, Gosani, — here is Vrindavana.

**201.** See — the thickets of ishika and munja reeds, wondrously lovely:
here suddenly [the boys] could not see the cows.

**202.** Not seeing the cows, He blew on the flute —
and the cows, pricking up their ears, come running, heads raised.

**203.** The cows run with grass in their mouths, the calves to the udders;
beasts and birds are enchanted by the song of the flute.

**204.** Again the boys were alarmed by a forest fire;
[Krishna] drank up the fire, [while] the boys closed their eyes[^48-15].

**205.** Thus Krishna's pastimes, place by place,
Gaura sees with joy," says Lochana.

[^48-1]: That is, though He is Krishna Himself, the Lord seems not to know His places and questions Krishnadasa.
[^48-2]: Vv. 88–95 — cf. Murari 4.04. Vasudeva carries the newborn Krishna from the prison to Gokula; the "place where He passed water" (mutra-sthana) is a shrine of Mathura.
[^48-3]: Uddhava is Krishna's friend and counsellor in Mathura; Krishna sent him to Vraja with a message to the gopis (Bhagavata 10.46–47). The Lord speaks as Krishna Himself, from memory.
[^48-4]: The Vishranti-ghata is the "ghata of rest", where Krishna rested after killing Kamsa; Gatashrama ("He whose weariness is gone") is the Deity of Vishnu at this ghata. Vv. 101–114 — cf. Murari 4.04: the houses of the washerman, the garland-maker, Kubja, Vasudeva and Ugrasena, the arena, the twelve ghatas, Kamsa's well.
[^48-5]: V. 128+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; it is absent from the 1903 edition.
[^48-6]: Vv. 116–130 — cf. Murari 4.05. Radha playfully teases Krishna: in the play of love the gopis deliberately refuse to acknowledge His greatness. According to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, Krishna is the original Bhagavan, and Rama is His manifestation; in answer to Her mockery He builds the dam. The Setubandha-sarovara is a lake near Kamana (Kamyavana).
[^48-7]: Vv. 132–136 — shrines of Mathura; cf. Murari 4.04. The Saptasamudraka-kunda is the "pond of the seven seas"; by tradition, at this place Kamsa killed Devaki's children. Patala is the underworld.
[^48-8]: Vv. 142–158 are a brief retelling of the Bhagavata (10.1–10.11): Narada's prophecy, Krishna's appearance in four-armed form, Vasudeva carries Him to Gokula, Yogamaya in the form of Yashoda's daughter goes up into the sky, the slaying of Putana, Shakata, Trinavarta, the universe in the Child's mouth, the stealing of butter, the Damodara-lila, the Yamala-arjuna trees, the fruit-seller. The she-jackal showing the ford is a Bengali tradition.
[^48-9]: Gopeshvara is Shiva, who by tradition took the form of a gopi to enter the rasa dance. Ayana (Abhimanyu) is by tradition Radha's husband; Gaudiya Vaishnavas teach that this marriage is only an appearance created by yogamaya for the sake of parakiya-rasa. "The cowherd Sundara" — so in A and C; the 1983 edition reads "Nanda-gopa". On "Ravana's forest of austerity" the 1903 edition gives in a footnote the reading: "where Ravana of Lanka performed austerities."
[^48-10]: Vv. 164–168 — a tradition (known from the Garga-samhita and the Brahma-vaivarta Purana): Nanda entrusts the infant Krishna to Radha in the Bhandira forest (in Lochana Dasa, in Bilvavana). Lochana Dasa tells it briefly and frankly. According to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching (Bhaktisiddhanta), the love of Radha and Krishna is wholly transcendental (aprakrita): Krishna is ever Radha's Beloved, and His pastimes in the form of a child are not to be measured by worldly standards; these lilas must not be understood after the pattern of worldly relations.
[^48-11]: V. 175+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; it is absent from the 1903 edition.
[^48-12]: Vv. 159–176 — cf. Bhagavata 10.11 (the move to Vrindavana because of the troubles in Gokula). Bhadra and Bhandira are two of the twelve forests.
[^48-13]: The Lord, in the mood of a cowherd friend (sakhya-bhava), calls Shridama and Sudama, and also the cows. In the 1983 edition the cows are Dhavali and Shangali (cf. en/23). "This is He — the hero of the Yadus": Krishnadasa recognizes in the Lord Krishna Himself.
[^48-14]: Vv. 190–200 follow the Bhagavata (10.11–10.20): Aghasura, the stealing of the calves by Brahma, Dhenuka, Kaliya, the forest fire, Pralamba. Pralamba was killed by Balarama in the Bhandira forest; "the death of Aghasura" in v. 200 (so in A and B) is apparently a slip for Pralamba.
[^48-15]: Vv. 201–204 follow the Bhagavata (10.19): the cows strayed into the reed thickets, and a forest fire broke out; Krishna told the boys to close their eyes and swallowed the fire.

---

# Shesha-khanda, part 4 (portion 49)

*The section "The Lord Sees Vrindavana" (continued): Krishnadasa shows the Lord the places of Krishna's pastimes — the stealing of the clothes, Govardhana, the places of the nauka-lila and the dana-lila, the ponds; the ashoka grove blossoms at the Lord's glance; at the place of the rasa the Lord is in ecstasy, and Krishnadasa tells the rasa-lila (with passages found only in the 1983 edition)*

*Raga shri; disha: "Ah, my wondrous Gora! People say: a youth of pure gold"*

**206.** Here the cowherd maidens kept a vow;
they desired: "Let us become maidservants at Krishna's feet"[^49-1].

**207.** Leaving their clothes and ornaments on this ghata,
they entered the water and bathe naked.

**208.** Suddenly Hari, taking the clothes and ornaments,
climbed a kadamba tree and laughs softly.

**209.** The maidens praise Him with great effort;
pleased, He gave them back their clothes and ornaments.

**210.** [Here] He praised Vrindavana, addressing the boys;
[here] He asked food of the wives of the sacrificing brahmanas and ate.

**211.** Through Kamsa's outrages all the cowherds, in fear,
went and took shelter on the hill Nandishvara.

**212.** They settled on both banks of the Manasa-Ganga,
and He sported on the summit of Govardhana.

**213.** Contending with Indra, He holds up this mountain:
the great hill He lifted in His seventh year.

**214.** The stream of the Manasa-Ganga lies north-east of the hill;
there is no dry land, and the gopis cannot cross.

**215.** Ferrying them in a boat, He increases the fun,
and in the midst of the water the gopis gave themselves to Him as a gift[^49-2].

**216.** Through the hill runs a highroad:
the people of Gokula and Mathura come and go on it.

**217.** Here, see, on the hill is a lovely place:
here He exacts the great toll from the gopis.

**218.** Sitting on this very rock He exacted the toll;
this is the toll-collector's platform, Lord — see it before You"[^49-3].

**219.** Seeing the rock, the Lord spoke in a faltering voice,
and His whole body turned crimson.

**220.** With His own hand the Lord strokes the rock
and gazes fixedly at the place where [Krishna] sat.

**221.** Now He presses His chest to it, now He bows,
now He says: "Radha, why do you not pay Me My toll?"[^49-4]

**222.** His body powerless, the Lord falls to the ground,
then suddenly rises and embraces that stone.

**223.** Krishnadasa says: "Gosani, listen to me:
You will surely see all the places — do not be agitated.

**224.** See, east of the hill is this Kusuma-vana [the flower grove];
to its south is the place of the rasa circle."

**225.** As soon as he said this, Gora says: "Stop, stop!
Tell properly about the holy rasa circle."

**226.** "Radha and Krishna danced the rasa — this is that very place."
As soon as he said this, tears streamed from Gora's eyes.

**227.** "O Krishna! O Radha!" He says again and again;
from His reddened eyes flow countless streams.

**228.** Crying "The holy rasa circle!" He rolls on the ground,
now, raising both arms, He lets out loud roars.

**229.** Placing knee over knee He stands thrice-bent[^49-5],
and, saying "listen, listen," He tells of Radha and Krishna.

**230.** And again: "What more shall I say?" — and He laughs aloud:
"Right here Radha and Krishna danced the rasa!"

**231.** Seeing Gaura overwhelmed, Krishnadasa says:
"On the hill is the place of pastimes by the Radha-kadamba[^49-6].

**232.** See — the place of the Annakuta, where [the cowherds] worshipped Indra:
at this place Krishna stopped the worship of Indra.

**233.** In his pride King Indra forgot himself
and sent storm and rain upon the cowherd community.

**234.** See — on the hilltop is a Deity in that very form:
the Deity named Hariraya on the hill.

**235.** On Govardhana, on the southern side, dwells
[the Deity] named Gopalaraya: here are Krishna's pastimes.

**236.** Having crushed Indra's pride, He climbs to the hilltop:
here Indra performed the anointing of the King of kings[^49-7].

**237.** South of the hill is the pond that removes all sins;
above it, see, is the stone of anointing[^49-8].

**238.** See — on the hill are five more ponds:
the Brahma-kunda and the Rudra-kunda — the essence of all tirthas,

**239.** [and] the ponds named Indra-kunda, Surya-kunda and Moksha-kunda:
all the tirthas on earth rest in them.

**240.** Here, when [Nanda] bathed before breaking the fast on Dvadashi,
Varuna carried Nanda off in order to see Krishna.

**241.** Here is the Brahma-kunda where [the cowherds] were immersed — see, here is Vrindavana:
[here] the children [of Vraja] saw Krishna's glory with their own eyes[^49-9].

**242.** See — north of this pond is an ashoka grove;
hear a wonderful account about it.

**243.** On the full-moon day of Kartika, in the middle of the day,
the trees [here] burst into flower, and the whole land sees it."

**244.** Hearing this, the Lord gazes at the grove —
and at once the trees blossomed out of season.

**245.** Trees and creepers came into blossom, laden with fruit and flowers;
seeing this wonder, Krishnadasa speaks.

**246.** From Gora's body blows a breeze of wondrous fragrance.
Krishnadasa says: "Your sannyasa is only a disguise"[^49-10].

**247.** He prostrates on the ground and remains motionless.
"Speak, speak, speak!" Gaura says to Krishnadasa.

**248.** Krishnadasa says: "Gosani, listen:
in this Vrindavana Krishna performed the rasa play.

**249.** At the foot of this wish-fulfilling tree He played the flute —
and for sixteen kroshas around the gopis went mad.

**250.** Drawn by Krishna, the gopis lost their senses
and disregarded family, good conduct, shame, fear and honour.

**251.** All of them had their clothes and ornaments in disarray,
all their thoughts gone to Krishna, the call of Madana ringing in their hearts.

**252.** Enchanted by transcendental love[^49-11], the maidens of Vraja
came to Krishna and all gathered together.

**253.** Here, see, on the left — this Govindaraya"[^49-12].
As soon as He heard this, Gorachand was rapt in heart.

**254.** Ecstasy came again; His body was not His own;
waves of prema fill earth and sky.

**255.** With thunderous roars He rains the nectar of rasa;
beasts and birds go mad with the joy of love.

**256.** All the trees blossomed out of season,
the cuckoos sing sweetly, the bees grew intoxicated.

**257.** The Lord calls, crying "The flute!", and, praising the rasa,
says with a gentle smile: "Splendid, splendid!"

**258.** Now, [like] some gopi, He says: "You stay here!" —
now He speaks [like] someone in a dream[^49-13].

**259.** Now, suddenly startled, He embraces His own body;
His body seemed to melt, all His limbs streaming.

**260.** Now in a childlike rapture He dances with loud laughter;
Krishnadasa, overwhelmed, weeps, fallen at His feet:

**261.** "There is no one in the three worlds with fortune like mine:
by great fortune I have found my lost treasure!"

**262.** When at these words the Lord's outer awareness returned,
He asks Krishnadasa: "Tell Me, what happened then?"

**263.** "Here [Krishna] instructs the gopis about duty to family —
to discern the gopis' hidden devotion,

**264.** or to increase their love:
He heightens the bhava in their hearts in the proper course of rasa[^49-14].

**265.** 'O slender-waisted ones, why are you in the grove at night?
You were not afraid — why have you come here?

**266.** Your hearts long for another's husband — that is why [you came];
but I do not look at other men's wives or touch them.

**267.** Go home and serve your husbands:
a wife serves her own husband — this is the essence of dharma.

**268.** Be he sick or old, poor or ugly —
service to one's own husband is the very form of the highest dharma.

**269.** Go, go home, go, maidens of Vraja:
a chaste wife does not neglect her duty.

**270.** I keep the great dharma and never act unrighteously;
not understanding My heart, what have you done?'

**271.** Hearing this, the maidens swooned;
they stood motionless, like pictures on a wall.

**272.** Their breathing grew faint, none had words;
the fever of love consumed their bodies.

**273.** Now their breath comes fast from the heat of separation,
now their eyes stream, now their whole body trembles.

**274.** Sometimes they gaze fixedly at Krishna,
sometimes, from love's emotion, they cannot be still.

**275.** Overwhelmed by feeling, meaning to say one thing they say another,
and themselves speak what is in all their hearts:

**276.** 'He whose beauty and qualities enchant the world —
how then can we weak women keep our composure?

**277.** We are women of good families; we know only the vow of fidelity to family;
but the sound of the flute has broken that vow.

**278.** You seem to know nothing — and we [ourselves] do not know [how we came]:
by Your world-enchanting qualities You have drawn the women here.

**279.** You are the supreme Husband of [all] husbands, You are the Atmarama;
if one leaves You, a husband is no refuge — this is proven.

**280.** You are the delight of our soul; take Your delight in us;
where did You see us serving another's husband?[^49-15]

**281.** O refuge of husbands, Husband, shelter of all,
bliss, supreme bliss, full of every joy!'

**282.** Overflowing with feeling, the loving maidens speak the truth;
hearing these words of love, Krishna was filled with feeling.

**283.** He looked with a tender smile at all the gopis —
how much joy the gopis found no one knows.

**284.** All the gopis surrounded the Lord, the jewel of the Yadus,
like steady lightning flashing in a cloud.

**285.** Here took place this wondrous rasa play:
one gopi, one Krishna — such was its circle.

**286.** Like a garland strung of golden champakas and emeralds —
so was the circle.

**287.** As many gopis, so many Krishnas in this rasa circle:
on the ground of Vrindavana the market of the rasa opened.

**288.** At the wish-fulfilling tree are the two, Radha and Krishna:
Radha, the source of [all] the gopis, the cause of rasa[^49-16].

**289.** From Krishna there appeared Krishnas without end:
as many Radhas, so many Krishnas — such was the arrangement.

**290.** Over the market of the rasa the moon is the banner;
the cuckoo, as watchman, awakens Kama.

**291.** The bees are the market's music, the wares are youth,
the buyer is the best of connoisseurs of rasa, Madana-mohana.

**292.** Knowing the gopis' pure prema, Sri Hari
reveals His quality of being subject to His devotees.

**293.** In groups are the gopis, skilled dancers,
and the dancer in their midst is the Lord, the jewel of the Yadus.

**294.** Bangles, anklets, jewels and belts of bells jingle;
among them the sweet voice of the flute rings out bright.

**295.** Rabab, upanga, the song of the svaramandala,
mridanga, mandira cymbals, dampha, pakhavaj — sweet melodies.

**296.** And another wonder — here, see, at that place:
in this Vrindavana Krishna made Rai sovereign.

**297.** He placed divine sandal and a garland on Radha
and Himself praises Her together with the gopis.

**298.** Having anointed Her, He says: 'Listen, gopis:
from today Radha is sovereign in Vrindavana'[^49-17].

**299.** Thus the King of the Yadus sports in the rasa —
and suddenly none of the gopis can see Him.

**300.** Leaving them all, He went away taking one gopi with Him[^49-18];
here the gopis weep, flinging themselves on the ground.

**300+1.** 'Tulasi, malati, yuthi, I ask you:
have you seen Haladhara's brother going by this path?[^49-19]

**300+2.** Auspicious tulasi, sheltered at Krishna's feet,
have you seen Krishna, our life, the jewel of the Yadus?

**300+3.** Who has stolen my dark sapphire?' —
the cowherd girls wander in the deep forest.

**300+4.** 'Rama's younger brother, knowing our pride —
the stealer of hearts — where has He gone, leaving us all?

**300+5.** Listen, O yuthika and mallika!
Kadamba, have you seen Krishna?' the gopis ask.

**300+6.** Not finding Him, all the gopis, for His sake,
enact all of Krishna's lilas:

**300+7.** one became Putana, another became Kana
and, sucking at the breast, took the other's life;

**300+8.** one friend came in the form of the cart,
and another, taking Krishna's form, destroyed her;

**300+9.** then one friend came as Agha and Baka,
and another, as Krishna, slew her.

**301.** And the gopi who was with Him, leaning on His fondness,
says with a smile: 'I am too weary to walk;

**302.** carry me however You can.' Kanu says:
'Come, I will carry you on My shoulders.'

**302+1.** In the cool forest a sea [of joy] surged in her breast;
pulling her garment tight, she girds her waist with a silk cloth[^49-20].

**303.** Taking her in His arms, He carried her some distance further —
and suddenly became cruel to her too[^49-21].

**303+1.** At the moment when, placing her hand on Krishna's crest, she was about to mount [His shoulders],
at that very moment Gopinatha vanished.

**304.** Here He vanished from her,
and that gopi, distraught, weeps alone.

**305.** Having lost Krishna, all the other gopis
wander here, bewildered and frantic.

**306.** Distraught in separation, the gopis weep aloud;
hearing of it, grief swells in the heart.

**306+1.** Wandering, the other gopis
see their dear friend Radha weeping[^49-22].

**306+2.** At the sight of Radha the grief of all welled up,
and all together, flinging themselves down, began to weep;

**306+3.** weeping and weeping, all went mad
and, swooning, fell to the ground.

**307.** Here the gopis are absorbed in Krishna's deeds:
whatever Krishna did wherever, so they do,

**308.** acting out the same, all in just the same way —
all the gopis maddened, their hearts full of Krishna.

**309.** When the gopis thus swooned,
here Krishna then appeared to them[^49-23].

**310.** And again He performed this rasa play,
and again, in the festival of the rasa, the gopis' joy and delight.

**311.** Thus in joyful play the night passed;
their bodies limp with languor, their attire loosened.

**312.** Taking all the gopis, He went to the bank of the Yamuna
and, weary, slept in the gopis' laps.

**313.** Here is the Yamuna's water, the cool breeze —
and all the gopis sleep happily in Krishna's embrace.

**314.** Thus the auspicious night turned into a bright morning,
and the gopis bowed and went home."

**315.** Thus place after place Goraraya sees;
joyfully Lochana Dasa sings Gora's glories.

[^49-1]: Vv. 206–210 follow the Bhagavata (10.22–10.23): the maidens' vow to the goddess Katyayani, the stealing of the clothes, the praise of Vrindavana's trees, the wives of the brahmanas performing a yajna; cf. Murari 4.07. The stealing of the clothes is a transcendental (aprakrita) lila: Krishna fulfils the maidens' desire to belong to Him (cf. en/48, note 10).
[^49-2]: The nauka-lila — Krishna's boat pastime with the gopis on the Manasa-Ganga (cf. Murari 4.08). "Gave themselves as a gift" refers to the gopis' self-surrender; their love is transcendental (cf. en/48, note 10).
[^49-3]: The dana-lila: Krishna, like a toll-collector, stops the gopis on their way across Govardhana; the "Dana-ghati" and the "toll platform" are holy places of Govardhana.
[^49-4]: The Lord, in Krishna's mood, addresses Radha with the words of the dana-lila.
[^49-5]: Tribhanga — Krishna's pose with the flute, bent in three places.
[^49-6]: "Radha-kadamba" — so in A and B; apparently a kadamba tree on Govardhana associated with Radha's pastimes (C's note gives the variant "Kanu's kadamba").
[^49-7]: Vv. 232–236 follow the Bhagavata (10.24–10.27): Krishna stops the sacrifice to Indra and arranges the worship of Govardhana (Annakuta); Indra sends the deluge; Krishna lifts the hill; the humbled Indra anoints Krishna (Govinda-abhisheka). Hariraya (Hari-deva) and Gopalaraya are Deities on Govardhana; Gopala on Govardhana was revealed by Madhavendra Puri (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 4).
[^49-8]: "The stone of anointing" (shila ubatane) — the reading is unclear (B: "to its south"); apparently the stone where, by tradition, Krishna was rubbed with fragrant paste.
[^49-9]: Vv. 240–241 follow the Bhagavata (10.28): Varuna's servant carried off Nanda, who had entered the Yamuna before dawn on Dvadashi; Krishna brought him back and then, immersing the cowherds in the Brahma-hrada, showed them His abode.
[^49-10]: Krishnadasa recognises Krishna Himself in the Lord: the sannyasi is only His outward guise (cf. en/48, note 13).
[^49-11]: Vv. 248–252 follow the Bhagavata (10.29.1–8); cf. Murari 4.09. Lochana Dasa himself calls the gopis' "kama" transcendental (aprakrita). In the Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching (Chaitanya-charitamrita, Madhya 8; Bhaktisiddhanta), the gopis' "kama" is pure prema, the desire only to please Krishna, without a trace of worldly lust; Madana here is Krishna Himself, Madana-mohana.
[^49-12]: Govindaraya — the Deity of Govinda in Vrindavana, revealed by Rupa Gosvami.
[^49-13]: The Lord, absorbed in bhava, speaks the words of the participants in the rasa-lila. The 1983 edition reads: "now He says: 'Gopis, stay here!'"
[^49-14]: Vv. 263–270 follow the Bhagavata (10.29.18–27): Krishna tests the gopis. Lochana Dasa himself explains that He speaks of duty in order to reveal their hidden devotion and increase their love.
[^49-15]: Vv. 276–281 — cf. the gopis' reply in the Bhagavata (10.29.31–41): Krishna is the Atmarama, the Soul of all souls and the true Husband of all, so the gopis' love for Him is no breach of dharma in the worldly sense. Gaudiya Vaishnavas (Bhaktisiddhanta) stress that the parakiya-rasa of Vraja is wholly transcendental and is no model for imitation in the world.
[^49-16]: Vv. 285–298 — the rasa-lila (cf. the Bhagavata 10.33; Murari 4.09). Radha is the source from whom all the gopis expand (cf. Chaitanya-charitamrita, Adi 4); the 1983 edition reads: "the gopis are portions of Radha, for the sake of rasa." In v. 289 A and C read "as many Radhas, so many Krishnas," B "as many gopis, so many Krishnas." In the 1983 edition vv. 287–295 stand after v. 310.
[^49-17]: Radha's anointing as sovereign of Vrindavana (Vrindavaneshvari) is a Vraja tradition, told briefly by Lochana Dasa; it is not in the Bhagavata.
[^49-18]: Vv. 299–310 follow the Bhagavata (10.30–10.32): Krishna vanishes from the rasa circle, taking one gopi away; the gopis search for Him, question the trees and enact His lilas; He leaves the chosen gopi too and then appears to all again. In the Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition this chosen gopi is Radha.
[^49-19]: Vv. 300+1 — 300+9 are found only in the 1983 edition (not in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition of 1922); the 1903 edition gives only the first of them, in a different wording, in a footnote as the reading of one manuscript. Cf. the Bhagavata 10.30.4–23. Haladhara is Balarama.
[^49-20]: V. 302+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; it is absent in the 1903 edition. The first line is unclear (B: "मातिल पाथर बुको"); translated by the probable sense.
[^49-21]: In the 1983 edition the first line is worded differently: "The gopi wished to climb onto [His] shoulders"; v. 303+1 is found only there (absent in the 1903 edition). Cf. the Bhagavata 10.30.37–38.
[^49-22]: Vv. 306+1 — 306+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; they are absent in the 1903 edition. Here the chosen gopi is named Radha outright (cf. the Bhagavata 10.30.39–41).
[^49-23]: Cf. the Bhagavata 10.32.1–2. In the 1983 edition vv. 307–308 stand before v. 301.

---

# Shesha-khanda, part 5 (portion 50)

*The section "The Lord Sees Vrindavana" (conclusion): Krishnadasa shows the Lord the Khadira-vana, Kumuda-vana, Ambika-vana and Nandishvara and tells of Akrura, the killing of Kamsa and Krishna's coming to Mathura; the Lord blesses Krishnadasa; the residents of the Mathura-mandala see Krishna in Him; the Lord sets out for Nilachala*

*Raga vibhasa; disha: "O Hari! Just once, only once, have mercy, Goraraya!"*

**316.** "See — here within is the Khadira-vana:
[by this way] Radha went to sell curd and milk.

**317.** Here Krishna plotted with the boys:
'Give Her a fright — let Radha have a scare.'

**318.** Hiding in the forest, the boys raise a fearful noise;
terrified, Radha clings tightly to Krishna.

**319.** Embracing Radha, Krishna says, 'Ah, ah!' —
kisses Her and soothes Her with loving words.

**320.** Receiving Krishna's love, Radhika is rapt:
in love's languor Radha forgot Her home.

**321.** Here in the bower are delightful pastimes:
enchanted by prema, both [danced] the great rasa.

**322.** Here [Krishna] received the name Madana-Gopala"[^50-1].
Hearing this, Gora joyfully says: "Good, good!"

**323.** "See — in Kumuda-vana the deeds of Krishna:
here He played games with the boys.

**324.** Shridama and Subala, the two chief ones in the pasture —
in the boys' game against each other a quarrel arose then;

**325.** that is why this place is called Kondaliya [— 'the place of the quarrel'].
I have told of the pastimes in the forest named Kumuda.

**326.** See — the forest of Ambika on the bank of the Sarasvati:
here the cowherd men and women worship Hara and Gauri.

**327.** For mocking the sons of Angira
the vidyadhara Sudarshana was in a serpent's body;

**328.** to end the curse he swallowed Nanda —
[and] disgorged Nanda, touching Krishna's feet.

**329.** [Here is] the death of Shankhachuda, Kuvera's servant:
with a blow of the fist on his head [Krishna] took [his] jewel.

**330.** Seizing the bull Arishta by the horns and legs,
[Krishna] dashed him down in the pasture and killed him — blood gushed from his mouth.

**331.** At Narada's words Kamsa grew troubled
[and] bound Vasudeva and Devaki in fetters.

**332.** Keshi, Kamsa's follower, takes the form of a horse —
mighty, black: the sight of him strikes fear.

**333.** Thrusting His arm into his mouth [and] stopping his breath,
here Gopinatha slew Keshi.

**334.** An asura in the form of a ram steals the boys
and, closing it with a stone, keeps them in a mountain cave.

**335.** Killing Vyoma by dashing him down, [Krishna] brought back the boys
and, rid of the villain, plays joyfully[^50-2].

**336.** Then Nanda's house was at Nandishvara;
see — west of here is also the Kamyaka-vana.

**337.** See — the sliding stone: these cowherd boys
slide down it here in play morning and evening[^50-3].

**338.** North of Nandishvara is the Pavana-sarovara;
see — all around are posts for tying the calves.

**339.** In Mathura Kamsa gave his order to Akrura —
and he entered [this] village at dusk[^50-4].

**340.** All the thoughts he had on the way here
were fulfilled when he saw the marks of the lotus feet.

**341.** In this pasture, seeing both Rama and Krishna,
he prostrated on the ground, falling at Their feet.

**342.** They took him home with honour,
and at night he told all of Kamsa's design.

**343.** In the morning Nanda proclaimed to all —
the word went round: 'Let us go to Kamsa with tribute.'

**344.** Here Rama and Krishna mounted the chariot
and go to the king together with Akrura.

**345.** Here the gopis are dying of weeping:
in separation from Krishna they weep, flinging themselves down.

**346.** Fallen on the ground they weep, their hair dishevelled,
clothes and ornaments all in disarray — such became their attire.

**347.** Their weeping cannot be told in words:
like a lifeless body, their hands and feet lie still.

**348.** Through a messenger Krishna Himself consoles them:
'I shall come within a few days[^50-5].

**349.** All of you are as dear to Me as life —
that a body should live without its life cannot be.

**350.** When I have destroyed the wicked, I shall come soon;
do not grieve; know all this to be true.'

**351.** Here all the cowherds mounted the carts
and all rested at the ghata of the Manasa-Ganga.

**352.** They reached the Yamuna ghata at the middle of the third prahara;
all the cowherds bathed and ate fruit.

**353.** When Akrura bathed, [Krishna] showed [him His] glory;
in the afternoon Nanda and the others go ahead, and Krishna behind.

**354.** Here all the people cried out in dismay:
'He is no match for a wrestler — He is a mere boy!

**355.** Kamsa acts unfittingly, acts wrongly!'
As each one's heart was, so he saw Krishna[^50-6].

**356.** Kamsa was startled, in deep confusion:
in Krishna and Balarama he sees Death embodied.

**357.** The wrestlers see Him as if made of thunderbolt,
the yogis see Him as the complete Bhagavan,

**358.** the Yadus see Him as the deity of their clan,
the ignorant as the universal form of the Creator,

**359.** the cowherds see Him as their own kin,
the women see Kandarpa embodied.

**360.** When the two brothers stood in the arena,
each one saw there according to his own perception.

**361.** The two brothers fight with Chanura and Mushtika;
seeing it, the king starts again and again.

**362.** Krishna slew Chanura — the menace was ended;
Rama slew Mushtika — a crash like thunder.

**363.** And again with His fists He slays countless wrestlers;
the wrestler named Shala Krishna slew by dashing him down.

**364.** With a kick He broke one platform;
before Krishna's might the wrestlers flee in all directions.

**365.** Seeing all this, Kamsa quickly orders:
'Seize Rama and Krishna and throw Them out!

**366.** Put all the cowherds headed by Nanda in fetters,
kill Ugrasena, Vasudeva and Devaki!'

**367.** At that moment Krishnachandra, seeing the time had come,
with great daring leapt up onto the platform in one bound.

**368.** As Kamsa was hastily seizing his sword,
Krishna with a roar seized him by the hair.

**369.** Seizing him by the hair He threw him from the platform to the ground,
and the Vishvarupa — [the Lord whose form is the universe] — mounted his chest west of the platform.

**370.** Under the weight of the Vishvarupa Kamsa gave up his life.
Blessed is King Kamsa — Krishna is on his chest![^50-7]

**371.** He slew Kamsa — the people cry: 'Victory, victory!' —
and all the gods joyfully rain down flowers [on Him].

**372.** Holding him by the hair, Krishna dragged him along,
lifted him and, dashing him down, threw him some distance away.

**373.** Kamsa's eight brothers, headed by Kanka,
maddened by grief for their brother, gathered all their strength:

**374.** seven come on to kill Rama and Krishna —
Balarama alone slew them with a mere frown.

**375.** Through this village [Krishna] dragged Kamsa;
that is why [the place] is called Kamsa-khali — listen attentively.

**376.** [Here] He rested from His fatigue — hence the Vishranti-ghata;
Balarama consoles the lamenting wives of Kamsa[^50-8].

**377.** Then He set free His own mother and father;
overwhelmed with joy, they kiss [Him].

**378.** He made Ugrasena king and sent Nanda away [home] —
this is beyond my power to tell.

**379.** Krishna's hardness of heart is dreadful even to hear of[^50-9]:
in telling it he dies [of grief], says this Lochana Dasa.

*Raga bhatiyali; disha: "Come, come — I will take Kanai and Balai in my arms!"*

**379+1.** Then the father Vasudeva and the mother Devaki —
with the joy of the love of these two the earth was filled[^50-10].

**379+2.** He gave his sons the sacred thread and teaches [them] the gayatri;
for some time [They] pass Their days in pastimes in Mathura.

**379+3.** The tales of Krishna are almost without end;
there is no holding back — and the book grows too long.

**380.** Akrura tried to take [Them] to his house;
[Krishna] told him: "I shall come on the way back"[^50-11].

**381.** While Krishna lingered, the cowherds, near Mathura,
halted their carts there on the bank of the Sarasvati.

**382.** Leaving the cowherds headed by Nanda here,
Akrura himself goes ahead to inform Kamsa.

**383.** Understanding that they would stay here for a while,
the two brothers went to see Mathura.

**384.** They saw a washerman named Durmukha;
seeing him, Krishna and Balarama ask for clothes.

**385.** The sinful Durmukha speaks harsh words —
and [Krishna] struck off his head with His fingertips.

**386.** Putting on those fine clothes, happy and joyful,
They arrived at the house of Sudama the garland-maker.

**387.** Sudama rose, bowed to Their feet,
placed fine garlands on Their necks and offers praise.

**388.** Accepting his worship, the two brothers went on
and saw there a hunchbacked woman bent in three places.

**389.** Seeing the bent woman, [Krishna] laughed within
and in jest said to her: "Come, come."

**390.** Kubja with honour took both to her house
and anointed Their bodies with fragrant aloe and sandal.

**391.** Greatly pleased, Kubja paid Them honour —
and by the touch of the holy hand Kubja gained a beautiful form.

**392.** Senseless with desire, Kubja looks at Kanu
and, casting off shame, speaks openly[^50-12].

**393.** With words of assurance Hari pleased her,
and the two brothers went on in the attire of dancers.

**394.** Then at the place of the bow sacrifice [Krishna] broke the bow;
all Kamsa's men rushed to kill [Them],

**395.** but, taking the broken bow in hand, [They] slew Kamsa's men
and at dusk went to where Nanda and the others [were].

**396.** That very night Kamsa had evil dreams;
he built this very high platform.

**397.** Here, south of it, are two more platforms
for Vasudeva and Devaki to sit on:

**398.** "Tomorrow Rama and Krishna will come here and die —
let them sit here and see their sons' death!"

**399.** All around, all the ministers and friends set up platforms,
well arranged, to watch the wrestling in every detail.

**400.** To the west that villain dug a well,
to kill the two brothers and throw Them in it[^50-13].

**401.** Rising in the morning, King Kamsa sits on the platform:
"Bring all the cowherds — let them pay the royal dues!

**402.** Bring his two sons, Krishna and Balarama:
I have heard much of Them; I will watch Them fight."

**403.** At the king's order all the runners ran;
hearing the din of the contest, Rama and Krishna hasten [there].

**404.** They quickly went to the gate of the fort —
and at the gate of the fort stands an elephant the size of a mountain.

**405.** Seeing Rama and Krishna, it charges in fury to kill [Them];
angered, Krishna stood before it.

**406.** Seizing its trunk, in the struggle [He] climbs onto its neck,
kills the driver and wrenches the elephant's tusks.

**407.** Tearing out the tusks, He seizes it by the tail, whirls it round,
lifts it into the air and hurls it four yojanas away.

**408.** "The great elephant has fallen!" — King Kamsa hears;
his body began to tremble, fear in his heart.

**409.** Then Rama and Krishna went before the king;
all the cowherds tremble in fear, their hearts pounding.

**410.** Kamsa says to Chanura and Mushtika:
"I have a mind to watch a wrestling match."

**411.** Here took place the great combat of the wrestlers:
Krishna with Chanura, Balarama with Mushtika.

**412.** That Lord of Vrindavana [appeared] in the Kali age;
what He did then I shall tell — hear now[^50-14].

**413.** Gora circumambulated the Mathura-mandala;
the great Krishnadasa made everything known to Him.

**414.** He humbly entreats the Lord, falling at His feet:
"I am in great distress — do not go away, deceiving me!

**415.** You are that very Krishna — this I have known for certain;
show me grace, hear me, Goraraya!"

**416.** Hearing this, the Lord speaks:
"By your grace My mind has been purified.

**417.** I had a great desire to see Mathura —
by your grace I have seen the secret places.

**418.** As My heart has rejoiced,
[so] may Krishna be gracious to you, Krishnadasa!"

**419.** All the residents of the Mathura-mandala
all turned one way — to see Gaurachandra.

**420.** Whoever sees Him once cannot forget Him;
overwhelmed with prema, he cannot contain himself.

**421.** Children, old and young, women and men —
even the unlettered — say: "This is Krishna, this is Krishna!

**422.** At last Krishna has come to Mathura again
to see the secret places of His former [pastimes]."

**423.** Someone says: "Why does He stand thrice-bent?
If He is not Kanai, why does He call 'Radha!'?"[^50-15]

**424.** People stay with Him day and night and do not leave His side;
one by one the Lord looks at the trees of Vrindavana.

**425.** One by one the Thakura beholds all the places:
here, in the forests, all is filled with prema.

**426.** In the Mathura-mandala He is manifest in every house:
some see a Child, some the pastimes of a Youth.

**427.** Someone at home suddenly hears the sound of the flute;
for one, in her husband's embrace — the madness of Krishna-rasa[^50-16].

**428.** No one thinks Him a stranger — all say: "[He is] our own";
in the hearts of all the seed of prema sprouted.

**429.** When the Lord goes roaming in the forests,
the trees and creepers of that forest float in the flood of prema.

**430.** Cuckoos, bees, peacocks that roam the fields and pastures
come racing and stay near the Lord.

**431.** All, with upturned faces, look at the Lord's face;
equal tenderness for all, eyes full of prema.

**432.** All knew: He is a sannyasi only in disguise.
And Mahaprabhu, the dweller of Nilachala, set out on His way.

**433.** Here the account of the Mathura-mandala is concluded;
joyfully Lochana Dasa sings Gora's glories.

[^50-1]: Vv. 316–322 follow a Vraja tradition about the Khadira-vana. Madana-Gopala — "the Gopala [who conquers] Madana," the Deity of Krishna in Vrindavana (Madana-mohana). The love of Radha and Krishna is transcendental (aprakrita); cf. en/48, note 10.
[^50-2]: Vv. 323–335 follow the Bhagavata (10.34–10.37): the deliverance of the vidyadhara Sudarshana, who swallowed Nanda in Ambika-vana; the killing of Shankhachuda, Arishta, Keshi and Vyomasura; Narada reveals to Kamsa the secret of Krishna's birth. Cf. Murari 4.10.
[^50-3]: The "sliding stone" (phisalani-shila) is a holy place of Kamyavana; the Pavana-sarovara is a pond at Nandagrama (Nandishvara).
[^50-4]: Vv. 339–353 follow the Bhagavata (10.38–10.39): Akrura comes to Vraja on Kamsa's order, sees the marks of Krishna's feet, at night tells of Kamsa's design; Krishna and Balarama leave for Mathura; the gopis lament; in the waters of the Yamuna Akrura sees Vishnu on Ananta. Cf. Murari 4.11.
[^50-5]: Cf. the Bhagavata 10.39.35 and 10.46–10.47 (the message sent with Uddhava).
[^50-6]: Vv. 354–360 follow the Bhagavata (10.43.17): each one sees Krishna in the arena according to his own disposition. Vv. 356–360 are absent in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them in a footnote as an additional reading of one manuscript. In the 1983 edition vv. 380–411 (the way to Mathura, the washerman, Kubja, the bow, the elephant) stand before v. 354, which is more consistent; we keep Bhaktisiddhanta's order. Cf. Murari 4.12.
[^50-7]: According to the Bhagavata (10.44.39), Kamsa, who thought of Krishna incessantly, though in fear and enmity, attained liberation. Gaudiya Vaishnavas explain that enemies slain by the Lord receive liberation, but not prema.
[^50-8]: In the Bhagavata (10.44) it is Krishna who consoles Kamsa's wives.
[^50-9]: That is, that Krishna sent Nanda and the cowherds back to Vraja and Himself remained in Mathura (Bhagavata 10.45): for the Vaishnava poet Vraja's separation from Krishna is the bitterest thing of all.
[^50-10]: Vv. 379+1 — 379+3 are absent in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition (1922); they are found in the 1903 edition (here, after v. 379, with a new raga) and in the 1983 edition (before v. 412, since vv. 380–411 are moved earlier there). Cf. the Bhagavata 10.45 (Vasudeva performs the sacred-thread ceremony for his sons).
[^50-11]: Vv. 380–411 go back to the arrival of Krishna and Balarama in Mathura (Bhagavata 10.41–10.43): the washerman, Sudama the garland-maker, Kubja, the bow, the elephant Kuvalayapida, the challenge to the wrestling; cf. Murari 4.12. The washerman's name (Durmukha) is not given in the Bhagavata.
[^50-12]: Lochana Dasa speaks plainly of Kubja's desire. Gaudiya Vaishnavas (following Rupa Gosvami) distinguish Kubja's love, which contains a desire for her own pleasure (sadharani-rati), from the selfless love of the gopis (samartha-rati); cf. en/49, note 11.
[^50-13]: Vv. 396–400 (Kamsa's high platform, the platforms for Vasudeva and Devaki, the well) are details not found in the Bhagavata; cf. the Bhagavata 10.42.26–38 (evil omens, the preparation of the arena).
[^50-14]: Lochana Dasa returns to the story of Gaura: that same Krishna, the Lord of Vrindavana, appeared in the Kali age as Gaura.
[^50-15]: V. 423 is absent in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition puts it in brackets as the reading of some manuscripts.
[^50-16]: A spiritual experience is meant (the Lord revealing Himself in the heart), not a worldly feeling: the residents of the Mathura-mandala, seeing Gaura, experience Krishna's lilas — one sees the Child, another the Youth, another hears the flute.

---

# Shesha-khanda, part 6 (portion 51)

*The section "The Lord's Return to Nilachala" (ch. 3): mercy to the cowherd; the Lord at Kulia and Navadvipa — Shachi's lament and the Lord's instruction; kirtana at Advaita's house in Shantipura; by way of Tamoluk to Puri; mercy to King Prataparudra: the sannyasi in Jagannatha's place, the six-armed form, the instruction to the king*

## The Lord's Return to Nilachala

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* Having seen the places of Sri Krishna's lilas in the Mathura-mandala, Sriman Mahaprabhu wished to return to Nilachala. On the way He walked so fast that His companions could not keep up with Him and fell behind. The Lord was going alone along a forest path; meeting a cowherd boy on the way, He asked him for some buttermilk and, telling him to take the price of the buttermilk from the people coming behind, set off again. A little later the companions arrived there; the cowherd boy told them how the Lord had drunk the buttermilk and asked for payment — but the very next moment he saw that his pot was full of priceless treasure. Thus Sriman Mahaprabhu, as if for the sake of the buttermilk, showed mercy to this cowherd boy and, continuing on His way, came to Navadvipa. The people of Navadvipa ran like madmen to see the Lord. Mother Shachi came to the Lord weeping and began to tell Him the grief of separation. Then Mahaprabhu said to Mother Shachi: "I abide near whoever serves Krishna," and, instructing her in the service of Krishna, spent a day in the bliss of kirtana in the house of Acharya Gosani at Shantipura, and then by way of Tamoluk reached Nilachala and showed mercy to King Prataparudra.

*Raga suhai*

**1.** With a joyful heart the Lord goes to Nilachala;
crying "O Jagannatha!" He hastens [there] in love[^51-1].

**2.** The Lord goes in the bliss of prema with a lion's gait;
all who are with Him cannot keep pace.

**3.** Unable to keep up, the companions fell far behind,
and the Lord went on alone through the forest.

**4.** In the forest there is a village;
a cowherd youth is going to sell buttermilk.

**5.** The Thakura saw him — in His ecstasy He was thirsty:
"Give Me buttermilk, cowherd, I am thirsty."

**6.** Hearing this, the cowherd fell at His feet:
"Take the buttermilk — drink, Gosani, as much as You like."

**7.** He drank the buttermilk — the jar was empty;
having drunk the buttermilk, the sannyasi — a sannyasi only in guise — walks on.

**8.** He told the cowherd: "Wait here:
take the money from those who come behind."

**9.** Saying this, the Lord went off quickly,
and the cowherd, staying there, ponders within.

**9+1.** The cowherd thinks: "The sannyasi has told a lie," —
thinking so, the cowherd ponders long[^51-2]:

**9+2.** "When I go home, what shall I tell my family?
The sannyasi told a lie and went away."

**10.** After a while all the sannyasi's companions
come along that road, their minds absorbed in the Lord.

**11.** They asked: "Cowherd, did you see a sannyasi on the road?"
The cowherd says: "He drank a whole jar of buttermilk

**12.** and told me to take the money from you.
Give what is proper, and I will go home."

**13.** Hearing this, all look at one another;
all say: "Where would we have money?

**13+1.** We have with us neither a water-pot nor an outer cloth:
when we are thirsty, we drink water from cupped hands"[^51-3].

**14.** The cowherd said: "Go on, then there is no debt;
convey my obeisance to the sannyasi's feet."

**15.** Saying this, he takes the jar in his hands —
but the jar is very heavy; he cannot lift it onto his head.

**16.** Removing the lid, [he sees:] the jar is full of jewels —
and he ran off, crying "O sannyasi!"

**17.** A little way on, waiting for His lagging companions, stands the Lord;
seeing the cowherd, He smiles softly.

**18.** Then all the companions came up
and saw: the cowherd had attained the Lord's feet.

**19.** The Lord says: "Cowherd, go home:
Krishna has shown you grace; you have received a boon."

**20.** On his way back the cowherd received grace:
the cowherd goes about dancing in the madness of prema.

**21.** Seeing the cowherd, the joy of all increased;
happily this Lochana Dasa sings Gora's glories.

*Raga shyamagada; disha: "Ah, my, ah, my Gauranga-sundara! Under the weight of youthful prema He cannot walk"*

**22.** Thus step by step He comes along the road
and with His companions arrived in Gauda[^51-4].

**23.** Bathing in the Ganga, the Lord, by way of Radha-desha,
gradually arrived at the town of Kulia.

**24.** To see [the place of] one's former ashrama is a sannyasi's duty:
this is why He came near Navadvipa.

**25.** Hearing of the Lord's coming, the people of Navadvipa
came back to life, as it were, and forgot their grief.

**26.** Crying "O Gorachand!" they run [to Him] in love;
the ladies of good families run too, without looking back.

**27.** Shachi, beside herself, runs with upturned face,
her hair dishevelled, her cloth not drawn over her breast.

**28.** "Where is my Vishvambhara? Show him to my eyes!
I will kiss that beautiful face again.

**29.** My Nimai has come to Nadia!
People, seize him and keep him — there is no wrong in it.

**30.** He is the life of all; he alone is the soul:
without life, how can dharma be kept?"

**31.** Saying such things she came there
and saw where Gaurachandra was sitting.

**32.** Seeing the Lord, she says: "Listen, Nimai!
Come home — I have no use for [your] sannyasa.

**33.** By sannyasa you will keep dharma later,
but first the [sin of] my death will cling to you — everything else after."

**34.** Shachi, beside herself, weeps aloud
and gazes fixedly at His whole body.

**35.** Saying "My son, my son!" she wants to touch His body:
"Let all else be, my son," — and she lays her hand on Him.

**36.** "Dust has clung to the holy body — let me brush it off" —
saying this, she falls, flinging herself down.

**37.** And again, rising, she says: "My son, listen to me:
fulfil my heart's longing — let me hold you in my arms."

**38.** At the sight of Shachi's weeping the earth splits;
let alone people — even stones weep.

**39.** All the people around are distraught with weeping;
no one leaves His side; all have forgotten their homes.

**40.** Seeing the people weeping and His mother's distress,
the Lord considers: "What shall I say?"

**41.** Pondering within how to console His mother,
the Lord says sweetly: "Do not weep, do not weep!

**42.** You yourself gave permission to take sannyasa —
why then, overwhelmed, do you weep now?

**43.** The vain maya — [attachment to Me] as to a son — has not left you:
so hard to cast off is the maya of this dreadful samsara![^51-5]

**44.** Driven away, it does not go: so cruel is maya."
Shachi says: "Hear me, pitiless one!

**45.** You took birth on earth as my son
and made the people of the world honour me.

**46.** You are the friend of all, worshipped in the three worlds;
this 'maya' of Your affection I understand well from the scriptures.

**47.** Whatever becomes of me — be my son:
birth after birth may this thread of destiny remain mine."

**48.** At His mother's words the Lord was perturbed:
He cannot overcome her 'maya'; compassion overflows in Him[^51-6].

**49.** "Whatever you wish, do as pleases you;
I have told you only one last thing."

**50.** Shachi says: "You are going away, leaving Navadvipa —
and in Navadvipa Vishnupriya and I are grieving."

**51.** At His mother's word He went again to Navadvipa,
to the Barakona-ghata, near His own house.

**52.** He took alms in the house of Shuklambara Brahmachari
and in the morning, bowing to His mother, departed.

**53.** He told His mother: "I am captive to your virtues;
why have you forgotten the secret of the past?

**53+1.** I am Rama, Krishna, Vamana, Kapila and the rest:
look at all the births, considering everything[^51-7].

**53+2.** At all times such is My way:
I am near you — know this truth.

**53+3.** Now My descent is for the rasa of devotion:
except Krishnachandra, I shall worship nothing.

**54.** Be it a devotee, be it Vishnupriya, be it you —
whoever worships Krishna, I am in that one's embrace"[^51-8].

**55.** Bowing to His mother, the Lord says again and again:
"Do not abandon Krishna; do not serve this samsara."

**56.** Shachi's heart throbs;
the Thakura went; all the devotees run behind.

**57.** He went to Shantipura, to the Acharya's house;
in the joy of kirtana a day and a night passed.

**58.** Next morning the Lord again set out quickly:
His longing to see Jagannatha grew.

**59.** The Lord told everyone: "All of you go home;
I am in Nilachala" — so He answered.

**60.** "Whoever goes there to see Jagannatha —
there all will meet Me."

**61.** Saying this, the Lord cries "Hari-bol!"
The Thakura went — a sound of weeping rises.

**62.** Step by step He came to Tamoluk,
by the same way as He had come before.

**63.** The Lord goes along the road in the joy of prema's bliss;
the people along that road float in the rain of prema.

**64.** He goes laughing and playing, without the fatigue of the road,
and step by step reached Purushottama.

**65.** "I shall see Jagannatha, the King of Nilachala!" —
crying "O Jagannatha!" He hastens in love.

**66.** Coming to the Lion Gate, the Lord lets out a roar;
all the people found boundless joy.

**67.** Seeing Jagannatha, Goraraya was delighted;
seeing Him, the people find great happiness.

**68.** The people cry with loud voices: "Hari, Hari!"
and joyfully sing Hari's glories day and night.

**69.** Night and day the Lord delights in kirtana;
happily this Lochana Dasa sings Gora's glories.

*Raga lalita; disha: "Sing, sing of Gora's glories — the blessing of the whole world!"*

**70.** Joyful Mahaprabhu stays in Nilachala
and among the devotees performs the sankirtana of Hari's glories.

**71.** Many devotees gathered there;
every day Goraraya reveals Himself anew.

**72.** Now I shall tell how at that time
He, as He willed, showed mercy to Prataparudra[^51-9].

**73.** Hearing from people of Mahaprabhu's glories,
the king marvels but says nothing.

**74.** One day he went to see Jagannatha —
and does not see Jagannatha, but sees the best of sannyasis.

**75.** "What is this, what?" he ponders with an astonished heart
and asks the padichha: "What do you see, sir?"

**76.** The padichha says: "I see Lord Jagannatha."
The king says: "I keep you all in vain!

**77.** Look, in Jagannatha's place sits a sannyasi —
and you, for fear of my punishment, say you see nothing [else].

**78.** As if my eyes deceived me — that is never so!
Or else — what do you see? Tell the truth."

**79.** Hearing this, the padichha says again:
"Except Jagannatha we see nothing"[^51-10].

**80.** Then Prataparudra ponders within:
"Why then do I see the sannyasi with my own eyes?

**81.** I have heard that this sannyasi's greatness is boundless;
I must look into the cause of this."

**82.** Having thought thus, the king went quickly —
went himself to where the best of sannyasis was.

**83.** He saw: in the garden the sannyasi, among His own,
tells of Vrindavana and cries: "Hari, Hari!"

**84.** Again, looking at Jagannatha once more,
he saw that sannyasi, as vast as Mount Sumeru.

**85.** Seeing this, the king was amazed at heart:
"This Jagannatha is that very sannyasi-avatara!"

**86.** Love grows in Prataparudra's heart,
and he went quickly to where the Glorious One was.

**87.** There is no one in the garden — the gathering has broken up;
the king pleadingly says to Govinda[^51-11]:

**88.** "How may I see the Gosani's feet?
Tell me the means for it, great one."

**89.** Govinda says: "King, do not be distressed:
you will not see Him here — now is not the time."

**90.** "When shall I come, tell me, fortunate one?" —
the king speaks pleadingly, and his love grows.

**91.** That day the king stayed in that town
and, seeing [the Lord's] companions, implores them all.

**92.** All the devotees, headed by Puri Gosani,
resolved to bring this to the Gosani's notice.

**93.** When two or four days had passed thus,
they all gathered together in the house of Kashi Mishra.

**94.** All the devotees took counsel together
and resolved: "We shall tell Him all together."

**95.** The next day Mahaprabhu, as it happened,
was in Kashi Mishra's house among His devotees.

**96.** The king's anxiety weighed on all their hearts,
and Puri Gosani addressed the Lord:

**97.** "I have one request, Gosani, but fear to say it;
I will speak without fear if I have permission."

**98.** The Thakura says: "Listen, Puri Gosani:
before Me you need never be afraid.

**99.** Say what you would say — I will hear your heart."
Puri Gosani says: "Will You grant my request?

**100.** On behalf of all the devotees headed by Kashi Mishra
I say these words:

**101.** Sri Jagannatha-deva dwells in Nilachala,
and King Prataparudra is His own servant.

**102.** He entreats us all in order to see Your feet:
if he has permission, he will come before those feet."

**103.** The Lord says: "Everyone, hear My word:
it is not a sannyasi's dharma to see a king.

**104.** I am a sannyasi, and he is a great king:
neither of us has any purpose in meeting."

**105.** Puri Gosani says: "Lord, hear me:
if the king hears this, he will lose his senses.

**106.** We have seen what his love is like:
if he hears such words, he will give up his life, O Glorious One.

**107.** Today is already the king's tenth day of fasting:
leaving everything, he lies [at the door] in hope of [Your] feet."

**108.** And again all say pleadingly:
"Seeing the king's anxiety, we are asking."

**109.** Hearing this, the Lord says:
"Bring the king — I am pleased with him."

**110.** Hearing this, all rejoiced;
they brought the king — and the Lord reveals Himself.

**111.** Seeing the Lord, the king bows;
overwhelmed with prema, the king forgets himself.

**112.** His body filled with thrills, his eyes brimming;
seeing Gora, he was steeped in prema.

**113.** Seeing the king, the Lord smiles gently,
and the king sees the six-armed form [He] reveals[^51-12].

**114.** Seeing the Six-armed One, he prostrates;
under the weight of love his body sways.

**115.** His body is powerless, tears stream from both eyes;
all around, the name of Hari resounds, touching the sky.

**116.** Seeing the six-armed form, Sri Prataparudra,
overwhelmed with bliss, floats in the ocean of prema.

**117.** His whole body bristles from head to foot;
in a faltering voice he calls: "Lord, Lord!"

**118.** Raising his arms he dances and cries: "Hari-bol!
My life is fulfilled — the Lord is pleased with me!"

**119.** The devotees all around float in bliss.
The Lord says: "King, hear My word:

**120.** to protect your subjects is your great dharma:
the subjects are sons, the king is father — such is the essence.

**121.** Only Krishna's mercy is equal to all living beings;
know by experience the nature of your own body:

**122.** be it king or subject, joy and sorrow are the same for all;
only by karma does a living being become lesser or greater.

**123.** Whoever, judging by himself, understands all others —
he indeed is Krishna's servant, I tell you"[^51-13].

**124.** Such an answer the Lord gave as instruction;
the king bows in exceptional bliss.

**125.** Hear, everyone, of Gorachand's manifestation;
joyfully this Lochana Dasa tells His glories.

[^51-1]: Vv. 1–21 — cf. Murari 4.13 ("Mercy to the Cowherd"). In Lochana Dasa, as in Murari (4.14–4.15), the Lord goes from Vrindavana through Bengal (Kulia, Navadvipa, Shantipura) to Puri; according to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 16–18, 25), the Lord visited Kulia and Shantipura on the way to Vrindavana and returned from Vrindavana by way of Prayaga and Kashi.
[^51-2]: Vv. 9+1 — 9+2 are found only in the 1983 edition; they are absent in the 1903 edition. The end of v. 9+1 is unclear; translated by the sense.
[^51-3]: V. 13+1 is found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives it in a footnote as an additional reading of one printed edition.
[^51-4]: Vv. 22–69 — cf. Murari 4.14–4.15 (Kulia, the meeting with Shachi, Advaita's house, the return to Puri).
[^51-5]: "Vain" (michha) — cf. en/37, note 14. The Lord speaks as a sannyasi-teacher, addressing the whole world through His mother. Shachi's love itself is no worldly attachment: in the Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching Shachi is the Lord's eternal associate (Yashoda), and her motherly love (vatsalya-prema) is spiritual; Shachi herself answers that she knows this "maya of affection" of the Lord from the scriptures (v. 46), and the Lord admits that He is "captive to her virtues" (v. 53).
[^51-6]: "Maya" here is Shachi's motherly love, to which the Lord Himself submits (cf. v. 46).
[^51-7]: Vv. 53+1 — 53+3 are found only in the 1983 edition; the 1903 edition gives them in a footnote as an additional reading of a printed edition. The Lord reminds Shachi that she is His mother in many descents (as Kaushalya, Devaki and Yashoda, Aditi, Devahuti); in the Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching all avataras come from Krishna, who has appeared as Gaura.
[^51-8]: Cf. the Lord's farewell to Shachi in Murari (4.14).
[^51-9]: Vv. 70–125 — the mercy to Prataparudra; cf. Murari 4.16 (the king sees Gaura and Nityananda in kirtana and then, in dreams, as Krishna and Balarama). According to the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Madhya 11–14), the Lord long refused to meet the king, saying that a sannyasi should not see a king (cf. vv. 103–104), and received him during the Ratha-yatra, when the king, dressed as a simple Vaishnava, recited the Gopi-gita. Lochana Dasa's account is different.
[^51-10]: The padichha is the superintendent of the Jagannatha temple. The king sees Gaura in Jagannatha's place: Gaura is non-different from Jagannatha (cf. en/45, note 15, on the Daru-brahman; below, v. 85: "This Jagannatha is that very sannyasi-avatara").
[^51-11]: The garden (tota) is the Lord's residence in Puri; Govinda is His servant; Puri Gosani (v. 92) is Paramananda Puri. In the 1983 edition vv. 87–88 are in a different wording, one couplet longer: "There is no one in the garden… The king, beside himself, lost his senses. The king pleadingly says to Govinda: 'How may I see the Gosani's feet? Tell me the means for it, great one,' — so he says again and again."
[^51-12]: The six-armed form (shadbhuja) — by tradition, with the arms of Rama (bow and arrow), Krishna (flute) and the sannyasi (staff and water-pot). By tradition, the Lord also revealed the six-armed form to Sarvabhauma (en/45, vv. 243–245 and note 22).
[^51-13]: That is, whoever sees in others what he sees in himself — their joy and sorrow — is a true servant of Krishna.

---

# Shesha-khanda, part 7 (portion 52)

*The section "The Final Pastimes": the poor brahmana from Dravida and Vibhishana — the end of the text in Bhaktisiddhanta's edition. Chapter 4 (only in the 1983 edition): the concealment of the Lord — He enters into Jagannatha; the devotees' grief; Lochana Dasa's closing word about himself*

## The Final Pastimes (Shesha-lila)

> *Summary by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.* Shortly before Sriman Mahaprabhu withdrew [from mundane sight], a poor brahmana from Dravida, tormented by want, came to Purushottama and fasted seven days to obtain the mercy of Sri Jagannatha-deva; not obtaining it, he resolved to give up his life in the ocean. Then, on the ocean shore, by providence he met Vibhishana, and Vibhishana, instructing him: "A living being experiences joy and sorrow according to the fruits of his karma; therefore one should worship Jagannatha-deva, remaining indifferent to joy and sorrow," went on his way; but the brahmana did not leave him and together with Vibhishana came to Sriman Mahaprabhu. [Mahaprabhu, having granted him wealth through Vibhishana,] instructed him in [the service of] Krishna and showed him mercy.

*Raga varadi*
